@@@@ 素問 Suwen @@@@


1. 上古天真論 Discourse on the Natural Purity of Ancient Times

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
相關討論
	上古天真... :	
昔在黃帝，生而神靈，弱而能言，幼而徇齊，長而敦敏，成而登天。迺問於天師曰：余聞上古之人，春秋皆度百歲，而動作不衰；今時之人，年半百而動作皆衰者，時世異耶，人將失之耶？歧伯對曰：上古之人，其知道者，法於陰陽，和於術數，食飲有節，起居有常，不妄作勞，故能形與神俱，而盡終其天年，度百歲乃去。今時之人不然也，以酒為漿，以妄為常，醉以入房，以欲竭其精，以耗散其真，不知持滿，不時御神，務快其心，逆於生樂，起居無節，故半百而衰也。
		In ancient times there was Huangdi; he was born with divine wisdom, could speak even when young and weak, behaved wisely as a child, matured into diligence and intelligence, and at adulthood ascended to heaven. He then asked the Celestial Master: "I have heard that in ancient times, people lived for more than a hundred years and yet did not experience physical decline; why is it that nowadays, when people reach fifty, their physical functions already begin to decline? Is this due to differences in the times, or have human beings lost something?" Qibo replied: "In ancient times, those who understood the Dao followed the principles of yin and yang, harmonized with techniques and numbers, maintained regular eating and drinking habits, kept a consistent daily routine, did not overexert themselves unnecessarily, so they could maintain both body and spirit in harmony, thus living out their full natural lifespan, reaching more than a hundred years before passing away. People nowadays are not like that. They treat wine as water, regard recklessness as routine, indulge in sexual activity while drunk, exhaust their essence through desire, dissipate their true energy, do not know how to maintain fullness, fail to regulate their spirit at appropriate times, pursue only immediate pleasure, go against the joy of life, and have no regularity in their daily habits. Hence they begin to decline by fifty."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
相關討論
	上古天真... :	
夫上古聖人之教下也，皆謂之虛邪賊風，避之有時，恬惔虛无，真氣從之，精神內守，病安從來。是以志閑而少欲，心安而不懼，形勞而不倦，氣從以順，各從其欲，皆得所願。故美其食，任其服，樂其俗，高下不相慕，其民故曰朴。是以嗜欲不能勞其目，淫邪不能惑其心，愚智賢不肖不懼於物，故合於道。所以能年皆度百歲，而動作不衰者，以其德全不危也。
		The teachings of the sage rulers in ancient times instructed people to avoid harmful external influences, such as pathogenic qi and damaging winds, at appropriate times. By maintaining a tranquil and untroubled mind and embracing emptiness and non-action, true qi would follow naturally, with spirit and energy remaining internally protected—under these conditions, how could illness arise? Therefore, their aspirations were leisurely and desires few; their minds remained at peace without fear; their bodies worked but did not tire; qi flowed smoothly in accordance with nature. Each followed his own inclinations and attained what he wished for. Thus, they found satisfaction in their food, were content with their clothing, took joy in their customs, and did not envy those above or below them. Therefore, the people of that time were said to be simple and unpretentious. Therefore, desires could not weary their eyes, licentiousness and evil influences could not confuse their minds, and whether foolish or wise, virtuous or unworthy, they were untroubled by external things—thus they conformed to the Dao. This is why they could all live beyond a hundred years, with their physical functions not declining: because their virtue was complete and free from danger.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
相關討論
	上古天真... :	
帝曰：人年老而無子者，材力盡邪，將天數然也。歧伯曰：女子七歲，腎氣盛，齒更髮長；二七而天癸至，任脈通，太衝脈盛，月事以時下，故有子；三七，腎氣平均，故真牙生而長極；四七，筋骨堅，髮長極，身體盛壯；五七，陽明脈衰，面始焦，髮始墮；六七，三陽脈衰於上，面皆焦，髮始白；七七，任脈虛，太衝脈衰少，天癸竭，地道不通，故形壞而無子也。丈夫八歲，腎氣實，髮長齒更；二八，腎氣盛，天癸至，精氣溢寫，陰陽和，故能有子；三八，腎氣平均，筋骨勁強，故真牙生而長極；四八，筋骨隆盛，肌肉滿壯；五八，腎氣衰，髮墮齒槁；六八，陽氣衰竭於上，面焦，髮鬢頒白；七八，肝氣衰，筋不能動，天癸竭，精少，腎藏衰，形體皆極；八八，則齒髮去，腎者主水，受五藏六府之精而藏之，故五藏盛，乃能寫。今五藏皆衰，筋骨解墮，天癸盡矣。故髮鬢白，身體重，行步不正，而無子耳。帝曰：有其年已老而有子者何也。歧伯曰：此其天壽過度，氣脈常通，而腎氣有餘也。此雖有子，男不過盡八八，女不過盡七七，而天地之精氣皆竭矣。帝曰：夫道者年皆百數，能有子乎。歧伯曰：夫道者能卻老而全形，身年雖壽，能生子也。
		The emperor asked: "When a person grows old without having children, is it because their physical strength has been exhausted, or is it simply the natural order of things?" Qibo said: "At seven years old, a girl's kidney qi becomes strong; her teeth change and her hair grows longer; at fourteen (two sevens), the heavenly water arrives, the Ren meridian becomes unblocked, and the Taichong pulse is strong; menstrual cycles occur regularly, so she can bear children; at twenty-one (three sevens), kidney qi reaches balance, thus the true teeth grow and reach full length; at twenty-eight (four severs), sinews and bones become strong, hair grows to its fullest, and the body reaches its prime of strength; at thirty-five (five sevens), the Yangming meridian declines, the face begins to wither, and hair starts to fall out; at forty-two (six sevens), the three yang meridians decline in the upper body, the face becomes dry and withered, and hair begins to turn white; at forty-nine (seven sevens), the Ren meridian becomes deficient, the Taichong pulse weakens and declines, heavenly water dries up, the lower pathways become blocked, so the body deteriorates and she is unable to bear children. Men at eight years old have strong kidney qi; their hair grows longer and their teeth change; at sixteen (two eights), kidney qi is strong, heavenly water arrives, essence and qi overflow and are discharged, yin and yang harmonize, so they can father children; at twenty-four (three eights), kidney qi reaches balance; sinews and bones become firm and strong, so the true teeth grow to full length; at thirty-two (four eights), sinews and bones are at their peak strength, muscles are full and robust; at forty (five eights), kidney qi declines, hair falls out and teeth become dry and brittle; at forty-eight (six eights), yang qi declines in the upper body; the face withers, hair and temples turn gray; at fifty-six (seven eights), liver qi declines, sinews can no longer move freely; heavenly water dries up; essence becomes scarce; the kidney organ weakens, and both body and form reach their decline; at sixty-four (eight eights), teeth and hair fall out. The kidneys govern water, receiving the essence of the five zang and six fu organs to store it; therefore, only when the five zang are strong can there be discharge of essence. Now, all five zang organs have declined, sinews and bones loosen and weaken, and heavenly water is completely exhausted. Therefore, hair and temples turn white, the body becomes heavy, gait becomes unsteady, and there are no children." The emperor asked: "What is the reason that some people who have already grown old still have children?" Qibo said: "This is because their natural lifespan has not yet reached its end, their qi and meridians remain unblocked, and their kidney qi remains abundant." Although they may still have children, men will not exceed the age of sixty-four (eight eights) and women will not surpass forty-nine (seven sevens); at these ages, both heaven and earth's essence and qi are completely exhausted. The emperor asked: "Those who follow the Dao live to be over a hundred years old—can they still have children?" Qibo said: "Those who follow the Dao can resist aging and preserve their physical form. Though their lifespan is long, they are still able to father children."

4
打開字典
相關討論
	上古天真... :	
黃帝曰：余聞上古有真人者，提挈天地，把握陰陽，呼吸精氣，獨立守神，肌肉若一，故能壽敝天地，无有終時，此其道生。中古之時，有至人者，淳德全道，和於陰陽，調於四時，去世離俗，積精全神，游行天地之間，視聽八達之外，此蓋益其壽命而強者也，亦歸於真人。其次有聖人者，處天地之和，從八風之理，適嗜欲於世俗之間，无恚嗔之心，行不欲離於世，被服章，舉不欲觀於俗，外不勞形於事，內无思想之患，以恬愉為務，以自得為功，形體不敝，精神不散，亦可以百數。其次有賢人者，法則天地，象似日月，辯列星辰，逆從陰陽，分別四時，將從上古合同於道，亦可使益壽而有極時。
		Huangdi said: "I have heard that in ancient times there were True Persons who could lift up heaven and earth, grasp yin and yang, breathe in essence and qi, stand alone while guarding their spirit, with flesh and muscles unified as one. Thus they could live as long as heaven and earth endure, without end—this is the Dao of life." In the middle ancient times, there were Supreme Persons who possessed pure virtue and complete understanding of the Dao. They harmonized with yin and yang and regulated themselves according to the four seasons. They withdrew from worldly affairs and cultivated their essence to preserve their spirit. Traveling freely between heaven and earth, they could see and hear beyond the eight directions. These individuals extended their lifespans and strengthened their bodies; thus, they also belonged among the True Persons. Next were the Sages, who dwelled in harmony with heaven and earth and followed the principles of the eight winds. They adjusted their desires to fit within worldly customs, harbored no resentment or anger, did not wish to separate from society, wore appropriate clothing, and acted without seeking admiration from 俗 convention. Externally, they did not exhaust themselves through worldly affairs; internally, they were free from burdens of thought. Taking tranquility and joy as their purpose, and personal contentment as their achievement, their bodies remained unimpaired and their spirits undispersed—thus, they too could live to be over a hundred. Next were the Virtuous Persons, who followed the laws of heaven and earth, emulated the sun and moon, distinguished the stars and constellations, understood yin and yang in their movements, differentiated the four seasons, and aspired to unite with the Dao of ancient times. They too could extend their lifespans, though within defined limits. 

2. 四氣調神大論 Great Discourse on Regulating Spirits According to the Four Seasons

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	四氣調神... :	
春三月，此謂發陳，天地俱生，萬物以榮，夜臥早起，廣步於庭，被髮緩形，以使志生，生而勿殺，予而勿奪，賞而勿罰，此春氣之應養生之道也。逆之則傷肝，夏為寒變，奉長者少。夏三月，此謂蕃秀，天地氣交，萬物華實，夜臥早起，無厭於日，使志無怒，使華英成秀，使氣得泄，若所愛在外，此夏氣之應養長之道也。逆之則傷心，秋為痎瘧，奉收者少，冬至重病。秋三月，此謂容平，天氣以急，地氣以明，早臥早起，與雞俱興，使志安寧，以緩秋刑，收斂神氣，使秋氣平，無外其志，使肺氣清，此秋氣之應養收之道也，逆之則傷肺，冬為飱泄，奉藏者少。冬三月，此謂閉藏，水冰地坼，無擾乎陽，早臥晚起，必待日光，使志若伏若匿，若有私意，若已有得，去寒就溫，無泄皮膚使氣亟奪，此冬氣之應養藏之道也。逆之則傷腎，春為痿厥，奉生者少。
		In spring, the third month is called "Fa Chen," when heaven and earth both come to life, and all things flourish. One should go to bed late and rise early, take leisurely walks in the courtyard, let one's hair down and relax one's body, so as to promote the growth of one's will. Encourage growth without killing, give freely without taking away, reward without punishing—this is how one should respond to the spring qi and follow the way of preserving health. Going against this principle will injure the liver, leading in summer to cold-related disorders, and one's ability to support growth will be diminished. In summer, the third month is called "Fan Xiu," when the energies of heaven and earth intermingle, and all things bloom and bear fruit. One should go to bed late, rise early, not be weary of the sun, keep one's will calm without anger, allow the flowers and buds to flourish, let the energy flow freely outward as if one's love were directed outside—this is how one responds to summer qi and follows the way of promoting growth and development. Going against this principle will injure one's heart, leading in autumn to chills and fever (malaria-like symptoms), reducing the capacity for harvest; by winter solstice, serious illness may occur. In autumn, the third month is called "Rong Ping," when the energy of heaven becomes urgent and that of earth clear. One should go to bed early and rise early, in accordance with the rooster's crowing; keep one's will tranquil to ease the severity of autumn, gather inwardly both spirit and qi, so that autumn's energy remains balanced; do not let one's thoughts stray outward, thus keeping the lung qi pure—this is how one responds appropriately to autumn qi and follows the way of preservation and gathering. Going against this principle injures the lungs, leading in winter to diarrhea, and reducing the capacity for storage. In winter, the third month is called "Bi Cang," when water freezes and the earth cracks. Do not disturb yang energy; go to bed early and arise late, waiting until sunlight appears. Keep one's will subdued as if hidden away, as though harboring private thoughts or having already gained something. Avoid cold by seeking warmth, and do not expose the skin excessively so that qi is not rapidly depleted—this is how one appropriately responds to winter qi and follows the way of conservation and storage. Going against this principle injures the kidneys, leading in spring to atrophy and cold extremities, with diminished capacity for growth and renewal.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	四氣調神... :	
天氣，清淨光明者也，藏德不止，故不下也。天明則日月不明，邪害空竅，陽氣者閉塞，地氣者冒明，雲霧不精，則上應白露不下。交通不表，萬物命故不施，不施則名木多死。惡氣不發，風雨不節，白露不下，則菀稾不榮。賊風數至，暴雨數起，天地四時不相保，與道相失，則未央絕滅。唯聖人從之，故身無奇病，萬物不失，生氣不竭。逆春氣，則少陽不生，肝氣內變。逆夏氣，則太陽不長，心氣內洞。逆秋氣，則太陰不收，肺氣焦滿。逆冬氣，則少陰不藏，腎氣獨沈。夫四時陰陽者，萬物之根本也。所以聖人春夏養陽，秋冬養陰，以從其根，故與萬物沈浮於生長之門。逆其根，則伐其本，壞其真矣。
		Heaven's qi is pure, clean, and luminous; when virtue is stored without ceasing, it does not descend. When heaven's clarity is obscured, the sun and moon become dim; malevolent influences harm empty orifices. Yang qi becomes blocked, while earth's energy rises upward in confusion. When clouds and mists are not pure, this corresponds above to a failure of white dew to descend. When the interplay between heaven and earth is obstructed and not properly expressed, all living things suffer, for life cannot be transmitted; when life transmission ceases, even great trees will die. When harmful qi does not disperse, winds and rains are irregular, and white dew fails to descend, then withered grasses do not flourish. When harmful winds frequently arrive and heavy rains often occur, the harmony between heaven, earth, and the four seasons is disrupted; when this occurs, one departs from the Dao, and life will be cut short before its time. Only a sage follows this principle; thus, his body is free of strange illnesses, all things remain in harmony, and vital energy does not run dry. Going against spring qi causes the lesser yang to fail to generate, leading to internal changes in liver qi. Going against summer qi causes greater yang to fail to grow, resulting in an inner emptiness of heart qi. Going against autumn qi causes greater yin to fail to gather inwardly, leading the lung qi to become dry and congested. Going against winter qi causes lesser yin to fail in storage, resulting in kidney qi sinking alone. The four seasons and their yin-yang energies are the fundamental basis of all things. Therefore, sages nourish yang in spring and summer, and yin in autumn and winter, following the root of this principle. Thus they rise and fall with all things through the gate of growth and renewal. Going against its root is to cut down the foundation, damaging one's true nature.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	四氣調神... :	
故陰陽四時者，萬物之終始也，死生之本也，逆之則災害生，從之則苛疾不起，是謂得道。道者，聖人行之，愚者佩之。從陰陽則生，逆之則死，從之則治，逆之則亂。反順為逆，是謂內格。是故聖人不治已病，治未病，不治已亂，治未亂，此之謂也。夫病已成而後藥之，亂已成而後治之，譬猶渴而穿井，鬭而鑄錐，不亦晚乎。
		Therefore, yin and yang and the four seasons are the beginning and end of all things, the root of life and death. Going against them brings disaster; following them prevents serious illness—this is called attaining the Dao. The Dao: sages follow it, while fools ignore and neglect it. Following yin and yang brings life; going against them leads to death. Following them brings order; going against them causes chaos. Reversing the natural order is called "ni," which means internal disharmony or rebellion. Therefore, a sage does not treat illness after it has arisen but prevents it before it occurs; does not address disorder after it has erupted but prevents it before it arises—this is what is meant by that. When illness has already formed and then one administers medicine, or when disorder has already arisen and then one attempts to manage it, is this not like digging a well in the midst of thirst or forging an awl during a fight—surely too late? 

3. 生氣通天論 Shengqi Tongtian Lun: The Discourse on Vital Qi and Its Connection to Heaven

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	生氣通天... :	
黃帝曰：夫自古通天者生之本，本於陰陽天地之間，六合之內，其氣九州九竅五藏十二節，皆通乎天氣。其生五，其氣三，數犯此者，則邪氣傷人，此壽命之本也。蒼天之氣清淨，則志意治，順之則陽氣固，雖有賊邪，弗能害也，此因時之序。故聖人傳精神，服天氣，而通神明。失之則內閉九竅，外壅肌肉，衛氣散解，此謂自傷，氣之削也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: Since ancient times, the vital essence of life is connected to heaven. This connection originates from the interplay of yin and yang between heaven and earth, within the 六合 (the six directions or universe). The qi of the nine provinces, the nine orifices, the five zang organs, and the twelve joints all communicate with the atmospheric conditions of heaven. There are five manifestations of life and three types of qi. Those who repeatedly violate these principles will suffer from pathogenic influences that harm the body; this is the fundamental basis of longevity and lifespan. When the qi of heaven is pure and clean, one's will and intentions remain in order. Following this principle strengthens yang energy, making it impervious to harmful pathogenic influences; this is in accordance with the natural sequence of time. Therefore, a sage preserves his spirit and energy, harmonizes with the atmospheric conditions of heaven, and thus attains communion with divine wisdom. If one fails to maintain this harmony, the nine orifices will become internally blocked and the muscles externally obstructed; the defensive qi will disperse and dissipate. This is called self-inflicted harm, a depletion of vital energy.

2
打開字典
	生氣通天... :	
陽氣者若天與日，失其所，則折壽而不彰，故天運當以日光明。是故陽因而上，衛外者也。因於寒，欲如運樞，起居如驚，神氣乃浮。因於暑，汗煩則喘喝，靜則多言，體若燔炭，汗出而散。因於濕，首如裹，濕熱不攘，大筋緛短，小筋弛長，緛短為拘，弛長為痿。因於氣，為腫，四維相代，陽氣乃竭。
		Yang qi is like the sky and the sun; if it loses its proper place, life will be shortened and vitality diminished. Therefore, the movement of heaven should manifest through the brightness of the sun. For this reason, yang rises upward by nature and serves to protect the exterior of the body. If one is affected by cold, it is as if a door hinge becomes stuck; movement and rest become disrupted, and the spirit and qi become unsettled. If one is affected by heat, perspiration increases, leading to panting and thirst; even when at rest, the person speaks excessively. The body feels as hot as burning charcoal, and sweat emerges profusely to dissipate the heat. If one is affected by dampness, the head feels as if wrapped in something heavy. If damp-heat remains unresolved, the large tendons become contracted and shortened, while the small tendons become relaxed and elongated; contraction and shortening lead to stiffness (拘), whereas relaxation and elongation result in atrophy or weakness (痿). If one is affected by qi stagnation, swelling occurs. When the four directions of movement alternate repeatedly, yang energy becomes exhausted.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	生氣通天... :	
陽氣者，煩勞則張，精絕辟積，於夏使人煎厥。目盲不可以視，耳閉不可以聽，潰潰乎若壞都，汨汨乎不可止。陽氣者，大怒則形氣絕，而血菀於上，使人薄厥。有傷於筋縱，其若不容，汗出偏沮，使人偏枯。汗出見濕，乃生痤疿。高梁之變，足生大丁，受如持虛。勞汗當風，寒薄為皻，鬱乃痤。
		Yang qi, when overexerted or burdened by excessive toil, becomes overly active; this leads to the depletion of essence and stagnation. In summer, it can cause a condition known as "Jianjue," characterized by heat-induced collapse. The eyes become blind and unable to see, the ears closed and unable to hear; it is as if a ruined capital lies in ruins, with confusion and disorder that cannot be stopped. Yang qi, when one experiences intense anger, causes the form and qi to become severed; blood stagnates in the upper part of the body, leading to a condition known as "Baojue," or sudden fainting due to upward rushing qi. If there is injury to the tendons, they become relaxed and lax; the body feels as if it cannot bear itself, with perspiration occurring unevenly on one side of the body, which can lead to hemiplegia or partial paralysis. When sweat is exposed to dampness, boils and skin eruptions arise. Excessive consumption of rich or greasy foods can lead to the formation of large abscesses on the feet; such conditions develop as if into a hollow vessel, easily invaded by pathogens. Exertion followed by perspiration and exposure to wind causes cold to invade the skin, resulting in rashes; if this stagnates, it leads to boils.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	生氣通天... :	
陽氣者，精則養神，柔則養筋。開闔不得，寒氣從之，乃生大僂。陷脈為瘻，留連肉腠。俞氣化薄，傳為善畏，及為驚駭。營氣不從，逆於肉理，乃生癰腫。魄汗未盡，形弱而氣爍，穴俞以閉，發為風瘧。故風者，百病之始也，清靜則肉腠閉拒，雖有大風苛毒，弗之能害，此因時之序也。故病久則傳化，上下不并，良醫弗為。故陽畜積病死，而陽氣當隔，隔者當寫，不亟正治，粗乃敗之。
		Yang qi, when refined, nourishes the spirit; when gentle and harmonious, it nourishes the tendons. If the body's openings and closings (such as pores or channels) do not function properly, cold qi will invade, leading to severe hunchback or curvature of the spine. When blood vessels become depressed and stagnant, it results in sores that linger within the flesh and skin layers. lø If the qi at the 俞 (shu) points becomes weakened or impaired, it may develop into a condition of constant fearfulness, and eventually lead to sudden frights or panic. If the nutritive qi does not flow properly but instead becomes obstructed within the muscle tissues, it gives rise to abscesses and swellings. If perspiration has not fully dissipated, the body becomes weak and the qi is scorched; the acupoints become closed off, leading to the onset of wind-induced malaria. Therefore, wind is the beginning of all diseases. When one maintains inner tranquility and stillness, the muscle tissues remain closed and resistant; even if exposed to strong winds or harmful toxins, they cannot cause harm. This is in accordance with the sequence of natural time. Therefore, when a disease persists for a long time, it spreads and transforms; if the conditions above and below do not harmonize, even an excellent physician cannot treat it effectively. Therefore, if yang accumulates excessively and becomes stagnant, the condition can lead to death; when yang qi is obstructed, it should be purged. If treatment is not promptly administered with proper methods, a careless or unskilled physician will worsen the condition.

5
打開字典
	生氣通天... :	
故陽氣者，一日而主外，平旦人氣生，日中而陽氣隆，日西而陽氣已虛，氣門乃閉。是故暮而收拒，無擾筋骨，無見霧露，反此三時，形乃困薄。
		Therefore, yang qi governs the exterior of the body throughout the day. At dawn, human qi begins to rise; at noon, yang reaches its peak; by dusk, yang becomes weakened and the pores close. Therefore, in the evening one should conserve and protect the body, avoiding disturbance to the tendons and bones, and not exposing oneself to mist or dew. To act contrary to these three times of day will lead to physical exhaustion and weakness.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	生氣通天... :	
歧伯曰：陰者，藏精而起亟也，陽者，衛外而為固也。陰不勝其陽，則脈流薄疾，并乃狂。陽不勝其陰，則五藏氣爭，九竅不通。是以聖人陳陰陽，筋脈和同，骨髓堅固，氣血皆從。如是，則內外調和，邪不能害，耳目聰明，氣立如故。
		Qibo said: Yin stores essence and supports the body's internal functions; yang protects the exterior and provides stability. When yin cannot withstand excessive yang, the blood vessels become agitated and the flow becomes rapid and shallow, leading to mania or madness. When yang cannot overcome yin, the five zang organs' qi contend with one another, resulting in blockage of the nine orifices. Therefore, a sage regulates yin and yang so that tendons and vessels harmonize, bones and marrow become strong, and qi and blood flow in accordance. In this way, the interior and exterior of the body remain harmonized; pathogenic influences cannot cause harm, the ears and eyes become sharp and clear, and vital energy remains stable as before.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
相關討論
	生氣通天... :	
風客淫氣，精乃亡，邪傷肝也。因而飽食，筋脈橫解，腸澼為痔。因而大飲，則氣逆。因而強力，腎氣乃傷，高骨乃壞。凡陰陽之要，陽密乃固，兩者不和，若春無秋，若冬無夏，因而和之，是謂聖度。故陽強不能密，陰氣乃絕，陰平陽秘，精神乃治，陰陽離決，精氣乃絕，因於露風，乃生寒熱。
		When wind invades and disturbs the qi excessively, essence is lost; this is a pathogenic injury to the liver. If one then consumes excessive food, the tendons and vessels become relaxed and loose, leading to intestinal disorders and hemorrhoids. If one indulges in excessive drinking, it causes reversal of qi flow. If one overexerts physically, the kidney qi is injured, and the vertebrae or spinal column may be damaged. The essential principle of yin and yang is that when yang is properly sealed, the body remains stable. If yin and yang are disharmonious, it is like having spring without autumn or winter without summer; only by harmonizing them can one attain the wisdom of a sage. Therefore, if yang is excessive and cannot be properly sealed, yin will be depleted. When yin remains balanced and yang is well contained, the spirit and qi remain in harmony; when yin and yang separate completely, essence and qi are cut off. Exposure to wind or cold may then give rise to alternating chills and fever.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	生氣通天... :	
是以春傷於風，邪氣留連，乃為洞泄。夏傷於暑，秋為痎瘧。秋傷於濕，上逆而欬，發為痿厥。冬傷於寒，春必溫病。四時之氣，更傷五藏。
		Therefore, if one is harmed by wind in spring, pathogenic qi lingers within the body, eventually leading to diarrhea with loose stools or even dysentery. Injury from heat in summer may manifest as malaria-like symptoms in autumn. Injury from dampness in autumn causes upward reversal of qi and coughing, which can develop into atrophy or cold extremities (wēijué). Injury from cold in winter will inevitably lead to a warm disease in spring. The qi of the four seasons alternately harms the five zang organs.
