打開字典
顯示相似段落
	生氣通天... :	
陰之所生，本在五味，陰之五宮，傷在五味。是故味過於酸，肝氣以津，脾氣乃絕。味過於鹹，大骨氣勞，短肌，心氣抑。味過於甘，心氣喘滿，色黑腎氣不衡。味過於苦，脾氣不濡，胃氣乃厚。味過於辛，筋脈沮弛，精神乃央，是故謹和五味，骨正筋柔，氣血以流，湊理以密，如是，則骨氣以精，謹道如法，長有天命。
		Yin is generated from the Five Flavors; however, the Five Palaces of Yin (the internal organs) are harmed by excessive consumption of these same flavors. Therefore, if sour flavor is consumed in excess, the liver qi becomes excessive, and the spleen qi may be cut off or impaired. Therefore, excessive salty flavor causes exhaustion of the great bones' qi, shortens the muscles, and suppresses heart qi. Excessive sweet flavor causes the heart's qi to become labored and full, darkens the complexion, and disrupts the balance of kidney qi. Excessive bitter flavor dries out the spleen's qi, making the stomach's qi thickened or congested. Excessive pungent flavor causes the tendons and vessels to become dry and lax, and impairs mental clarity. Therefore, one must carefully balance the Five Flavors so that bones remain straight, tendons supple, qi and blood flow smoothly, and the skin pores remain tightly sealed. In this way, bone and qi will be refined; by strictly following these principles with care, one may enjoy a long life in harmony with heaven's will. 

4. 金匱真言論 Jin Kui Zhenyan Lun

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	金匱真言... :	
黃帝問曰：天有八風，經有五風，何謂。歧伯對曰：八風發邪，以為經風，觸五藏，邪氣發病。所謂得四時之勝者，春勝長夏，長夏勝冬，冬勝夏，夏勝秋，秋勝春，所謂四時之勝也。東風生於春，病在肝，俞在頸項；南風生於夏，病在心，俞在胸脇；西風生於秋，病在肺，俞在肩背；北風生於冬，病在腎，俞在腰股；中央為土，病在脾；俞在脊。故春氣者病在頭，夏氣者病在藏，秋氣者病在肩背，冬氣者病在四支。故春善病鼽衄，仲夏善病胸脇，長夏善病洞泄寒中，秋善病風瘧，冬善病痺厥。故冬不按蹻，春不鼽衄，春不病頸項，仲夏不病胸脇，長夏不病洞泄寒中，秋不病風瘧，冬不病痺厥飱泄，而汗出也。夫精者身之本也，故藏於精者春不病溫。夏暑汗不出者，秋成風瘧。此平人脈法也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "Heaven has eight winds, and the meridians have five winds; what are these?" Qibo replied: "The eight winds give rise to pathogenic influences, which become meridian winds and affect the five zang organs, causing illness due to evil qi." What is meant by "gaining the dominance of the four seasons" is that spring dominates long summer, long summer dominates winter, winter dominates summer, summer dominates autumn, and autumn dominates spring. This is what is referred to as the dominance of the four seasons. The east wind originates in spring; illness occurs in the liver, and the shu points are located in the neck and nape of the neck; The south wind originates in summer; illness occurs in the heart, and the shu points are found in the chest and hypochondrium; The west wind originates in autumn; illness occurs in the lungs, and the shu points lie on the shoulders and back; The north wind originates in winter; illness occurs in the kidneys, and the shu points reside in the waist and thighs; The center corresponds to earth; illness occurs in the spleen; the shu points are located along the spine. Therefore, illnesses caused by spring qi affect the head; those caused by summer qi affect the zang organs; those caused by autumn qi affect the shoulders and back; and those caused by winter qi affect the four limbs. Therefore, in spring people are prone to rhinorrhea and epistaxis; in midsummer they are prone to illnesses of the chest and hypochondrium; in long summer they are prone to diarrhea with clear discharges and cold in the middle jiao; in autumn they are prone to wind-induced malaria; and in winter they are prone to bi syndrome and 厥 (jue). Therefore, in winter there is no need to treat injuries from falls; in spring there will be no rhinorrhea or epistaxis, and no illness of the neck and nape; in midsummer there will be no illness of the chest and hypochondriac regions; in long summer there will be no diarrhea with clear discharges or cold in the middle jiao in autumn there will be no wind-induced malaria; in winter there will be no bi syndrome, jue, dysentery, or excessive sweating. Essence is the fundamental substance of the body; therefore, those who store essence well will not suffer from febrile diseases in spring. Those who do not perspire during summer heat are likely to develop wind-induced malaria in autumn. This is the pulse method for a healthy person.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	金匱真言... :	
故曰：陰中有陰，陽中有陽。平旦至日中，天之陽，陽中之陽也；日中至黃昏，天之陽，陽中之陰也；合夜至雞鳴，天之陰，陰中之陰也；雞鳴至平旦，天之陰，陰中之陽也。故人亦應之。夫言人之陰陽，則外為陽，內為陰。言人身之陰陽，則背為陽，腹為陰。言人身之藏府中陰陽，則藏者為陰，府者為陽。肝心脾肺腎五藏，皆為陰。膽胃大腸小腸膀胱三焦六府，皆為陽。所以欲知陰中之陰，陽中之陽者何也。為冬病在陰，夏病在陽，春病在陰，秋病在陽，皆視其所在，為施鍼石也。故背為陽，陽中之陽，心也；背為陽，陽中之陰，肺也；腹為陰，陰中之陰，腎也；腹為陰，陰中之陽，肝也；腹為陰，陰中之至陰，脾也。此皆陰陽表裏內外雌雄相輸應也，故以應天之陰陽也。
		Therefore it is said: "Within yin there is yin, and within yang there is yang." From dawn until noon, this is the yang of heaven; it is yang within yang. From noon until dusk, this is also the yang of heaven but it is yin within yang. From nightfall until cockcrow, this is the yin of heaven; it is yin within yin. From cockcrow until dawn, this is also the yin of heaven but it represents yang within yin. Therefore, human beings correspond to these patterns as well. When speaking of the yin and yang of a person, the exterior is yang, and the interior is yin. When referring to the yin and yang within the human body, the back is yang, and the abdomen is yin. When discussing the yin and yang of the zang-fu organs within the human body the zang organs are yin, while the fu organs are yang. The liver, heart, spleen, lungs, and kidneys—the five zang organs—are all classified as yin. The gallbladder, stomach, large intestine, small intestine, bladder, and sanjiao—the six fu organs—are all classified as yang. Therefore, what is the reason for wanting to know about yin within yin and yang within yang? It is because illnesses occurring in winter are located in yin, those in summer in yang those in spring in yin and those in autumn in yang. One should determine the location of the illness and apply acupuncture or moxibustion accordingly. Therefore, the back is yang within yang, it is also yang—the heart. The back is yang yet within yang, it contains yin—the lungs. The abdomen is yin and within yin, it is also yin—the kidneys. The abdomen is yin yet within yin, it contains yang—the liver. The abdomen is yin; and within yin, the most yin of all—is the spleen. These are all examples of how yin and yang correspond in terms of exterior-interior, interior-exterior and feminine-masculine relationships. Therefore, they respond to the yin and yang patterns of heaven.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	金匱真言... :	
帝曰：五藏應四時，各有收受乎，歧伯曰：有。東方青色，入通於肝，開竅於目，藏精於肝，其病發驚駭，其味酸，其類草木，其畜雞，其穀麥，其應四時，上為歲星，是以春氣在頭也，其音角，其數八，是以知病之在筋也，其臭臊。南方赤色，入通於心，開竅於耳，藏精於心，故病在五藏，其味苦，其類火，其畜羊，其穀黍，其應四時，上為熒惑星，是以知病之在脈也，其音徵，其數七，其臭焦。中央黃色，入通於脾，開竅於口，藏精於脾，故病在舌本，其味甘，其類土，其畜牛，其穀稷，其應四時，上為鎮星，是以知病之在肉也，其音宮，其數五，其臭香。西方白色，入通於肺，開竅於鼻，藏精於肺，故病在背，其味辛，其類金，其畜馬，其穀稻，其應四時，上為太白星，是以知病之在皮毛也，其音商，其數九，其臭腥。北方黑色，入通於腎，開竅於二陰，藏精於腎，故病在谿，其味鹹，其類水，其畜彘，其穀豆，其應四時，上為辰星，是以知病之在骨也，其音羽，其數六，其臭腐。故善為脈者，謹察五藏六府，一逆一從，陰陽表裏，雌雄之紀，藏之心意，合心於精，非其人勿教，非其真勿授，是謂得道。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "Do the five zang organs correspond to the four seasons, each having its own functions and receptivity?" Qibo replied: "Yes." The East is associated with the color blue; it connects inwardly to the liver, opens into the eyes stores essence in the liver. Its illnesses manifest as startlement and fear. Its flavor is sour, its category includes grasses and trees, its domestic animals are chickens, its grains are wheat, and its correspondence to the four seasons is represented above by the planet Jupiter. Therefore, spring qi resides in the head. Its musical tone is jue its number eight; thus we know that illness resides in the sinews. Its odor is 臊 (sao). The South is associated with the color red; it connects inwardly to the heart, opens into the ears stores essence in the heart. Therefore, its illnesses affect the five zang organs. Its flavor is bitter, its category corresponds to fire, its domestic animals are sheep, its grains are millet, and its correspondence to the four seasonal cycles is represented above by the star Mars. Thus we know that illness resides in the vessels (blood vessels). Its musical tone is shang its number seven; its odor is scorched. The center is associated with the color yellow; it connects inwardly to the spleen, opens into the mouth stores essence in the spleen. Therefore, its illnesses manifest at the base of the tongue. Its flavor is sweet, its category corresponds to earth, its domestic animals are cattle, its grains are j 稷 (ji), and its correspondence to the four-season cycle is represented above by the planet Saturn. Thus we know that illness resides within the flesh. Its musical tone is gong its number five; its odor is fragrant. The West is associated with the color white; it connects inwardly to the lungs, opens into the nose stores essence in the lungs. Therefore, its illnesses occur on the back. Its flavor is pungent, its category corresponds to metal, its domestic animals are horses, its grains are rice, and its correspondence to the seasonal cycle is represented above by the star Venus. Thus we know that illness occurs in the skin and hair. Its musical tone is shang, its number nine; its odor is fishy. The North is associated with the color black; it connects inwardly to the kidneys, opens into the two yin (the anus and urethra) stores essence in the kidneys. Therefore, its illnesses are located at the xī (small joints or creases). Its flavor is salty, its category corresponds to water, its domestic animals are pigs, its grains are beans, and its correspondence with the four seasons is represented above by Mercury. Thus we know that illness lies within the bones. Its musical tone is yu its number six; its odor is putrid. Therefore, those skilled in pulse diagnosis must carefully observe the five zang and six fu organs noting their patterns of disharmony and harmony. They should understand yin-yang, exterior-interior relationships as well as the principles of feminine and masculine forces. These insights should be internalized with mindfulness, harmonizing one's mind with essence. The art must not be taught to just anyone; it should only be passed on to those who are truly worthy. This is called attaining the Dao. 

5. 陰陽應象大論 Great Treatise on the Correspondences of Yin and Yang

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	陰陽應象... :	
黃帝曰：陰陽者，天地之道也，萬物之綱紀，變化之父母，生殺之本始，神明之府也，治病必求於本。故積陽為天，積陰為地。陰靜陽躁，陽生陰長，陽殺陰藏。陽化氣，陰成形。寒極生熱，熱極生寒。寒氣生濁，熱氣生清。清氣在下，則生飱泄，濁氣在上，則生䐜脹。此陰陽反作，病之逆從也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: Yin and Yang are the principles of heaven and earth, the framework for all things, the parents of change, the origin of life and death, and the repository of spiritual wisdom. In treating illness, one must always seek its root. Therefore, accumulated Yang becomes heaven, and accumulated Yin becomes earth. Yin is still while Yang is active; Yang promotes birth while Yin supports growth; Yang causes death while Yin brings concealment. Yang transforms into qi, and Yin forms substances. Extreme cold gives rise to heat, and extreme heat gives rise to cold. Cold generates turbidity, while heat generates clarity. When clear qi resides below, it causes diarrhea; when turbid qi resides above, it causes distension and fullness. This is the reversal of Yin and Yang, which determines whether a disease progresses or recedes.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	陰陽應象... :	
故清陽為天，濁陰為地；地氣上為雲，天氣下為雨；雨出地氣，雲出天氣。
		Therefore, clear Yang becomes heaven, while turbid Yin becomes earth; the qi of the earth rises as clouds, and the qi of heaven descends as rain; Rain arises from earthly qi, while clouds arise from heavenly qi.

3
打開字典
	陰陽應象... :	
故清陽出上竅，濁陰出下竅；清陽發腠理，濁陰走五藏；清陽實四支，濁陰歸六府。
		Therefore, clear Yang emerges through the upper orifices, and turbid Yin emerges through the lower orifices; lòng Clear Yang spreads to the skin and pores, while turbid Yin moves toward the five zang organs; clear Yang fills the four limbs, while turbid Yin returns to the six fu organs.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	陰陽應象... :	
水為陰，火為陽，陽為氣，陰為味。味歸形，形歸氣，氣歸精，精歸化，精食氣，形食味，化生精，氣生形。味傷形，氣傷精，精化為氣，氣傷於味。陰味出下竅，陽氣出上竅。味厚者為陰，薄為陰之陽。氣厚者為陽，薄為陽之陰。味厚則泄，薄則通。氣薄則發泄，厚則發熱。壯火之氣衰，少火之氣壯。壯火食氣，氣食少火。壯火散氣，少火生氣。氣味，辛甘發散為陽，酸苦涌泄為陰。
		Water is Yin, fire is Yang; Yang is qi, and Yin is flavor. Flavors nourish the body form, form depends on qi, qi gives rise to essence, and essence leads to transformation. Essence is sustained by qi, form is sustained by flavors; transformation produces essence, and qi produces form. Excess flavor harms the body, excess qi harms essence; essence transforms into qi, but excessive flavors harm qi. Yin flavors emerge through lower orifices, while Yang qi emerges through upper orifices. Strong flavors are Yin; mild flavors are the Yang aspect of Yin. Strong qi is Yang, while mild qi is the Yin aspect of Yang. Strong flavors cause diarrhea, while mild flavors promote smooth flow. Mild qi promotes dispersion and release, while strong qi generates heat. Excessive fire leads to decline in energy; moderate fire strengthens vitality. Excessive fire consumes qi, while qi depends on moderate fire. Excessive fire disperses qi; moderate fire generates qi. Flavors and properties: pungent and sweet, which disperse outward, belong to Yang; sour and bitter, which promote vomiting and purging, belong to Yin.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	陰陽應象... :	
陰勝則陽病，陽勝則陰病。陽勝則熱，陰勝則寒。重寒則熱，重熱則寒。寒傷形，熱傷氣。氣傷痛，形傷腫。故先痛而後腫者，氣傷形也；先腫而後痛者，形傷氣也。風勝則動，熱勝則腫，燥勝則乾，寒勝則浮，濕勝則濡寫。
		When Yin prevails, Yang becomes diseased; when Yang prevails, Yin becomes diseased. Excess Yang causes heat; excess Yin causes cold. Extreme cold generates heat, and extreme heat generates cold. Cold injures the body form; heat injures qi. Injury to qi causes pain, and injury to form causes swelling. Therefore, when pain occurs before swelling, it is due to injury of qi affecting the body; when swelling occurs before pain, it is due to injury to the body affecting qi. When Wind prevails, there is movement; when Heat prevails, there is swelling; when Dryness prevails, there is dryness; when Cold prevails, there is floating or distension; and when Dampness prevails, there are moist discharges.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	陰陽應象... :	
天有四時五行，以生長收藏，以生寒暑燥濕風。人有五藏，化五氣，以生喜怒悲憂恐。故喜怒傷氣，寒暑傷形。暴怒傷陰，暴喜傷陽。厥氣上行，滿脈去形。喜怒不節，寒暑過度，生乃不固。故重陰必陽，重陽必陰。故曰：冬傷於寒，春必溫病；春傷於風，夏生飱泄；夏傷於暑，秋必痎瘧；秋傷於濕，徘生欬嗽。
		Heaven has four seasons and five elements to regulate growth, flourishing, storage, and concealment; from these arise cold, heat, dryness, dampness, and wind. Humans have five zang organs that transform into five types of qi, giving rise to the emotions of joy, anger, sorrow, worry, and fear. Therefore, excessive joy or anger injures qi; cold or heat injures form. Sudden rage injures Yin, and sudden joy injures Yang. Rebellious qi rises upward, filling the vessels and leaving the body vulnerable. When joy and anger are not regulated, and when exposure to cold or heat is excessive, life becomes unstable. Therefore, extreme Yin must transform into Yang, and extreme Yang must transform into Yin. Hence it is said: Injury from cold in winter will lead to warm diseases in spring; injury from wind in spring will cause diarrhea in summer; injury from heat in summer will lead to malaria-like fevers in autumn; injury from dampness in autumn will give rise to coughing and wheezing.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
相關討論
	陰陽應象... :	
帝曰：余聞上古聖人，論理人形，列別藏府，端絡經脈，會通六合，各從其經，氣穴所發各有處名，谿谷屬骨皆有所起，分部逆從，各有條理，四時陰陽，盡有經紀，外內之應，皆有表裏，其信然乎。歧伯對曰：東方生風，風生木，木生酸，酸生肝，肝生筋，筋生心，肝主目。其在天為玄，在人為道，在地為化。化生五味，道生智，玄生神，神在天為風，在地為木，在體為筋，在藏為肝，在色為蒼，在音為角，在聲為呼，在變動為握，在竅為目，在味為酸，在志為怒。怒傷肝，悲勝怒；風傷筋，燥勝風；酸傷筋，辛勝酸。南方生熱，熱生火，火生苦，苦生心，心生血，血生脾，心主舌。其在天為熱，在地為火，在體為脈，在藏為心，在色為赤，在音為徵，在聲為笑，在變動為憂，在竅為舌，在味為苦，在志為喜。喜傷心，恐勝喜；熱傷氣，寒勝熱，苦傷氣，鹹勝苦。中央生濕，濕生土，土生甘，甘生脾，脾生肉，肉生肺，脾主口。其在天為濕，在地為土，在體為肉，在藏為脾，在色為黃，在音為宮，在聲為歌，在變動為噦，在竅為口，在味為甘，在志為思。思傷脾，怒勝思；濕傷肉，風勝濕；甘傷肉，酸勝甘。西方生燥，燥生金，金生辛，辛生肺，肺生皮毛，皮毛生腎，肺主鼻。其在天為燥，在地為金，在體為皮毛，在藏為肺，在色為白，在音為商，在聲為哭，在變動為欬，在竅為鼻，在味為辛，在志為憂。憂傷肺，喜勝憂；熱傷皮毛，寒勝熱；辛傷皮毛，苦勝辛。北方生寒，寒生水，水生鹹，鹹生腎，腎生骨髓，髓生肝，腎主耳。其在天為寒，在地為水，在體為骨，在藏為腎，在色為黑，在音為羽，在聲為呻，在變動為慄，在竅為耳，在味為鹹，在志為恐。恐傷腎，思勝恐；寒傷血，燥勝寒；鹹傷血，甘勝鹹。故曰：天地者，萬物之上下也；陰陽者，血氣之男女也；左右者，陰陽之道路也；水火者，陰陽之徵兆也；陰陽者，萬物之能始也。故曰：陰在內，陽之守也；陽在外；陰之使也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I have heard that the sage men of ancient times discussed human form, classified the zang and fu organs, mapped out meridians and collaterals, connected all directions of heaven and earth. Each followed its own channel; the locations where qi emerges from acupoints each had their names. The valleys and ridges associated with bones also had their origins. Their distribution in terms of direction and sequence was orderly. The patterns of Yin and Yang through the four seasons were fully structured, and the correspondences between exterior and interior all had clear relationships—this is truly so? Qibo replied: The East gives rise to Wind; Wind generates Wood; Wood produces sour flavor; the sour flavor nourishes the liver; the liver nourishes tendons; tendons give birth to the heart; and the liver governs the eyes. In heaven it is known as Xuan, in humans as Dao, and on earth as transformation. Transformation gives rise to the five flavors; Dao produces wisdom; Xuan produces spirit. Spirit, in heaven is Wind, on earth is Wood, in the body is tendons, in the zang organs is liver, in color is green, in tone is Jue, in sound is calling out, in movement is grasping, in orifices is eyes, in flavor is sour, and in emotion is anger. Anger injures the liver; sorrow overcomes anger; Wind injures tendons; dryness overcomes Wind; Sour flavor injures tendons; pungent flavor overcomes sour. The South gives rise to Heat; Heat generates Fire; Fire produces bitter flavor; the bitter flavor nourishes the heart; the heart nourishes blood; blood nourishes the spleen; and the heart governs the tongue. In heaven it is Heat, on earth Fire, in the body blood vessels, in zang organs the heart, in color red, in tone Shang, in sound laughter, in emotional movement worry, in orifices the tongue, in flavor bitter, and in emotion joy. Joy injures the heart; fear overcomes joy; Heat injures qi; Cold overcomes Heat. Bitter flavor injures qi; salty flavor overcomes bitter. The Center gives rise to Dampness; Dampness generates Earth; Earth produces sweet flavor; the sweet flavor nourishes the spleen; the spleen nourishes flesh; flesh nourishes the lungs; and the spleen governs the mouth. In heaven it is Dampness, on earth Earth, in the body flesh, in zang organs spleen, in color yellow, in tone Gong, in sound singing, in emotional movement rumination, in orifices mouth, in flavor sweet, and in emotion thinking. Thinking injures the spleen; anger overcomes thinking; Dampness injures flesh; Wind overcomes Dampness; Sweet flavor injures flesh; sour flavor overcomes sweet. The West gives rise to Dryness; Dryness generates Metal; Metal produces pungent flavor; the pungent flavor nourishes the lungs; the lungs nourish skin and hair; skin and hair nourish the kidneys; and the lungs govern the nose. In heaven it is Dryness, on earth Metal, in the body skin and hair, in zang organs lungs, in color white, in tone Shang, in expression weeping, in emotional movement sorrow, in orifices nose, in flavor pungent, and in emotion worry. Worry injures the lungs; joy overcomes worry; Heat injures skin and hair; Cold overcomes Heat; Pungent flavor injures skin and hair; bitter flavor overcomes pungent. The North gives rise to Cold; Cold generates Water; Water produces salty flavor; the salty flavor nourishes the kidneys; the kidneys nourish bone marrow; marrow nourishes the liver; and the kidneys govern the ears. In heaven it is Cold, on earth Water, in the body bones, in zang organs kidneys, in color black, in tone Yu, in sound groaning, in emotional movement trembling, in orifices ears, in flavor salty, and in emotion fear. Fear injures the kidneys; thinking overcomes fear; Cold injures blood; Dryness overcomes Cold; Salty flavor injures blood; sweet flavor overcomes salty. Therefore it is said: Heaven and earth are the upper and lower boundaries of all things; Yin and Yang are like the male and female aspects of blood and qi; Left and right are the pathways of Yin and Yang; Water and fire are the manifestations of Yin and Yang. Yin and Yang are the fundamental forces that initiate all things in the world. Therefore it is said: Yin within is the guardian of Yang; Yang without; is the messenger of Yin.

8
打開字典
	陰陽應象... :	
帝曰：法陰陽柰何。歧伯曰：陽勝則身熱，腠理閉，喘麤為之俛仰，汗不出而熱，齒乾以煩冤腹滿，死，能冬不能夏。陰勝則身寒汗出，身常清，數慄而寒，寒則厥，厥則腹滿，死，能夏不能冬。此陰陽更勝之變，病之形能也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: How should one follow the principles of Yin and Yang? Qibo said: When Yang prevails, the body becomes hot; the pores close; breathing becomes labored and coarse, causing bending forward or backward; sweat does not emerge despite heat; the teeth dry up, leading to distress and abdominal fullness—this condition is fatal. Those affected can endure winter but cannot survive summer. When Yin prevails, the body feels cold and perspires; it remains chilly constantly; shivering occurs frequently with a sensation of cold. Cold leads to cold extremities (厥), which in turn causes abdominal fullness—this condition results in death. Those affected can survive summer but cannot endure winter. This is the transformation caused by alternating dominance of Yin and Yang, manifesting as the nature and form of disease.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	陰陽應象... :	
帝曰：調此二者，柰何。歧伯曰：能知七損八益，則二者可調，不知用此，則早衰之節也。年四十，而陰氣自半也，起居衰矣。年五十，體重，耳目不聰明矣。年六十，陰痿，氣大衰，九竅不利，下虛上實，涕泣俱出矣。故曰：知之則強，不知則老，故同出而名異耳。智者察同，愚者察異，愚者不足，智者有餘，有餘則耳目聰明，身體輕強，老者復壯，壯者益治。是以聖人為無為之事，樂恬憺之能，從欲快志於虛无之守，故壽命无窮，與天地終，此聖人之治身也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: How can these two forces be regulated? Qibo said: If one understands the Seven Losses and Eight Benefits, then these two forces can be regulated. If one does not know how to apply this knowledge, it will lead to premature aging. At the age of forty, Yin qi naturally diminishes by half; vitality and daily functions begin to decline. At fifty years old, the body becomes heavy, and hearing and vision become impaired. At sixty years of age, Yin declines, qi is greatly weakened; the nine orifices become obstructed. The lower part becomes deficient while the upper part is excessive—thus, nasal discharge and tears flow freely. Therefore it is said: Understanding this leads to strength; ignorance of it brings aging. Thus, though the origin is the same, their names differ. The wise discern the commonalities, while the foolish focus on differences. The foolish are insufficient; the wise have abundance. Abundance brings sharp hearing and vision, a light and strong body, allowing the elderly to regain vigor and the vigorous to achieve even greater health. Therefore, the sage engages in effortless action and finds joy in tranquility. By following natural desires and cultivating contentment within emptiness and nothingness, they attain an endless lifespan, living in harmony with heaven and earth—this is how a sage maintains their body.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	陰陽應象... :	
天不足西北，故西北方陰也，而人右耳目不如左明也。地不滿東南，故東南方陽也，而人左手足不如右強也。帝曰：何以然。歧伯曰：東方陽也，陽者其精并於上，并於上，則上明而下虛，故使耳目聰明，而手足不便也。西方陰也，陰者其精并於下，并於下，則下盛而上虛，故其耳目不聰明，而手足便也。故俱感於邪，其在上則右甚，在下則左甚，此天地陰陽所不能全也，故邪居之。故天有精，地有形，天有八紀，地有五里，故能為萬物之父母。清陽上天，濁陰歸地，是故天地之動靜，神明為之綱紀，故能以生長收藏，終而復始，惟賢人上配天以養頭，下象地以養足，中傍人事以養五藏。天氣通於肺，地氣通於嗌，風氣通於肝，雷氣通於心，谷氣通於脾，雨氣通於腎。六經為川，腸胃為海，九竅為水注之氣。以天地為之陰陽，陽之汗，以天地之雨名之；陽之氣，以天地之疾風名之。暴氣象雷，逆氣象陽。故治不法天之紀，不用地之理，則災害至矣。
		Heaven lacks fullness in the northwest; therefore, the northwest is Yin. For this reason, human right ears and eyes are not as clear or sharp as those on the left side. The earth is incomplete in the southeast; therefore, the southeast is Yang. For this reason, a person's left hand and foot are not as strong as those on the right side. The Yellow Emperor asked: Why is it so? Qibo said: The East is Yang. Yang concentrates its essence upward; when the essence gathers above, the upper part becomes clear while the lower part remains deficient. This is why the ears and eyes become sharp, but the hands and feet are less agile. The West is Yin; Yin concentrates its essence downward. When the essence gathers below, the lower part becomes strong while the upper part remains deficient—thus, the ears and eyes are less sharp, but the hands and feet become more agile. Therefore, when both are affected by pathogenic influences, the upper part suffers more on the right and the lower part more on the left. This is a limitation of Yin and Yang in heaven and earth; thus, pathogens take residence there. Therefore, Heaven has essence, Earth has form; Heaven has eight chronological divisions, and Earth has five directions. Thus they become the parents of all things. Clear Yang rises to Heaven, and turbid Yin returns to Earth. Thus the movements and stillness of Heaven and Earth are governed by spiritual wisdom as their framework; therefore they can regulate growth, flourishing, storage and concealment in an endless cycle. Only the wise person matches Heaven above to nourish the head, follows the example of Earth below to nourish the feet, and aligns with human affairs on the sides to nurture the five zang organs. Heavenly qi communicates with the lungs; earthly qi communicates with the throat; Wind qi communicates with the liver; Thunder qi communicates with the heart; Grain qi communicates with the spleen; Rain qi communicates with the kidneys. The six meridians are like rivers, the intestines and stomach are like seas, and the nine orifices are like waterways through which qi flows. Taking Heaven and Earth as Yin and Yang, sweat—being Yang—is named after the rain of Heaven and Earth; Yang qi is named after the swift winds of Heaven and Earth. Sudden surges of qi resemble thunder, while rebellious qi resembles fire. Therefore, if treatment does not follow the principles of Heaven's order or apply the laws of Earth, calamities will arise.

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	陰陽應象... :	
故邪風之至，疾如風雨，故善治者治皮毛，其次治肌膚，其次治筋脈，其次治六府，其次治五藏。治五藏者，半死半生也。故天之邪氣，感則害人五藏；水穀之寒熱，感則害於六府；地之濕氣，感則害皮肉筋脈。故善用鍼者，從陰引陽，從陽引陰，以右治左，以左治右，以我知彼，以表知裏，以觀過與不及之理，見微得過，用之不殆。善診者，察色按脈，先別陰陽；審清濁，而知部分；視喘息，聽音聲，而知所苦；觀權衡規矩，而知病所主。按尺寸，觀浮沈滑濇，而知病所生；以治無過，以診則不失矣。故曰：病之始起也，可刺而已；其盛，可待衰而已。故因其輕而揚之，因其重而減之，因其衰而彰之。形不足者，溫之以氣，精不足者，補之以味。其高者，因而越之；其下者，引而竭之；中滿者，寫之於內；其有邪者，漬形以為汗；其在皮者，汗而發之；其慓悍者，按而收之；其實者，散而寫之。審其陰陽，以別柔剛，陽病治陰，陰病治陽，定其血氣，各守其鄉，血實宜決之，氣虛宜𤙲引之。
		Therefore, when pathogenic winds arrive, they strike as swiftly as wind and rain. Thus, the most skillful physician treats at the level of the skin and hair; next, at the muscles and skin; then, at tendons and vessels; followed by the six fu organs; lastly, the five zang organs. To treat the five zang organs means that one is half dead and half alive. Therefore, pathogenic influences from Heaven, when they affect a person, harm the five zang organs; the cold and heat of water and grains, when affecting a person, damage the six fu organs. The dampness from Earth, when it affects a person, harms the skin, flesh, tendons, and vessels. Therefore, a skilled acupuncturist draws Yang from Yin and Yin from Yang; treats the left with the right and the right with the left. By understanding oneself, one knows others; by observing the exterior, one understands the interior. One examines excesses and deficiencies to discern their principles, perceives subtle signs of imbalance, and thus applies treatment without danger. A skilled diagnostician observes complexion and palpates the pulse, first distinguishing Yin and Yang; examines clarity and turbidity to understand the regions of the body; observes breathing, listens to voice and sounds, and thereby knows what ails the patient; examines standards of measurement and principles of regulation to determine the root cause of illness. Palpates the cun-chi positions, observes floating, sinking, slippery, or 涩 pulses, and thereby understands where the disease originates; With treatment free of excess and diagnosis accurate, one will not err. Therefore it is said: When a disease first arises, it can be eliminated by acupuncture alone; when it becomes severe, one must wait for its decline before acting. Therefore, treat a mild condition by reinforcing it; reduce the excessive in a serious condition; and strengthen what is weakened when it declines. For those with insufficient form, warm them with qi; for those with deficient essence, nourish them with flavors. For conditions that are excessive above, guide the pathogenic factors outward to expel them; for those below, draw out and drain them completely; for fullness in the middle jiao, purge it from within. For those with pathogenic influences, induce sweating by soaking or bathing; for conditions on the skin, release them through perspiration. For those with aggressive and rapid qi, regulate and contain it by pressing; For actual conditions, disperse and purge them. Carefully examine Yin and Yang to distinguish the soft from the firm. When a Yang condition arises, treat it by regulating Yin; when a Yin condition arises, regulate Yang. Determine the state of blood and qi so that each remains in its proper domain. If blood is excessive, it should be drained; if qi is deficient, it should be supplemented and guided. 

6. 陰陽離合論 Yin-Yang Separation and Union Theory


1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	陰陽離合... :	
黃帝問曰：余聞天為陽，地為陰，日為陽，月為陰，大小月三百六十日成一歲，人亦應之。今三陰三陽，不應陰陽，其故何也。歧伯對曰：陰陽者，數之可十，推之可百，數之可千，推之可萬，萬之大不可勝數，然其要一也。天覆地載，萬物方生，未出地者，命曰陰處，名曰陰中之陰；則出地者，命曰陰中之陽。陽予之正，陰為之主。故生因春，長因夏，收因秋，藏因冬，失常則天地四塞。陰陽之變，其在人者，亦數之可數。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I have heard that Heaven is Yang, Earth is Yin, the sun is Yang, and the moon is Yin. The large and small months total three hundred and sixty days to make one year; human beings also correspond to this. Now, the Three Yins and Three Yangs do not correspond to Yin and Yang. What is the reason for this? Qibo replied: Yin and Yang—counted, they can be ten; extended, a hundred; counted, a thousand; extended, ten thousand. The vastness of ten thousand cannot be fully numbered, yet their essence is one. Heaven covers and Earth bears; all things thus come into being. Those not yet emerging from the earth are called Yin places, named as Yin within Yin; those that emerge from the earth are termed Yang within Yin. Yang provides the correctness, while Yin serves as the master. Therefore, birth occurs because of spring, growth because of summer, harvest because of autumn, and storage because of winter. If these regularities are lost, Heaven and Earth will become fourfold blocked. The transformations of Yin and Yang in human beings can also be counted numerically.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	陰陽離合... :	
帝曰：願聞三陰三陽之離合也。歧伯曰：聖人南面而立，前曰廣明，後曰太衝，太衝之地，名曰少陰，少陰之上，名曰太陽，太陽根起於至陰，結於命門，名曰陰中之陽。中身而上，名曰廣明，廣明之下，名曰太陰，太陰之前，名曰陽明，陽明根起於厲兌，名曰陰中之陽。厥陰之表，名曰少陽，少陽根起於竅陰，名曰陰中之少陽。是故三陽之離合也，太陽為開，陽明為闔，少陽為樞。三經者，不得相失也，搏而勿浮，命曰一陽。
		The Emperor said: I wish to hear about the separation and union of the Three Yins and Three Yangs. Qibo said: A sage stands facing south. In front is Guangming, and behind is Taichong. The area of Taichong is called Shaoyin; above Shaoyin is Taiyang. Taiyang originates at Zhiyin and terminates at Mingmen, and it is named Yang within Yin. From the middle of the body upward is called Guangming. Below Guangming is Taixue; in front of Taixue is Yangming, which originates at Liedui and is named Yang within Yin. The exterior of Jueyin is called Shaoyang, which originates at Qiaoyin and is named Lesser Yang within Yin. Therefore, the separation and union of the three Yangs: Taiyang is the opening, Yangming is the closing, and Shaoyang is the pivot. The three meridians must not be separated; they should converge without being superficial. This is called One Yang.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	陰陽離合... :	
帝曰：願聞三陰。歧伯曰：外者為陽，內者為陰，然則中為陰，其衝在下，名曰太陰，太陰根起於隱白，名曰陰中之陰。太陰之後，名曰少陰，少陰根起於涌泉，名曰陰中之少陰。少陰之前，名曰厥陰，厥陰根起於大敦，陰之絕陽，名曰陰之絕陰。是故三陰之離合也，太陰為開，厥陰為闔，少陰為樞。三經者，不得相失也。搏而勿沈，名曰一陰。陰陽𩅞𩅞，積傳為一周，氣裏形表而為相成也。
		The Emperor said: I wish to hear about the Three Yins. Qibo said: The exterior is Yang, the interior is Yin. Thus, the middle is Yin; its convergence lies below and is called Taixue. Taixue originates at Yinfen and is named Yin within Yin. Behind Taixue is Shaoyin, which originates at Yongquan and is named Lesser Yin within Yin. In front of Shaoyin is Jueyin, which originates at Dadun. This represents the extreme Yin within Yin, and it is called the utmost Yin in Yin. Therefore, regarding the separation and union of the Three Yin meridians: Taixue is the opening, Jueyin is the closing, and Shaoyin is the pivot. The three meridians must never be separated from one another. They should converge without sinking, and this is called One Yin. Yin and Yang circulate continuously; their accumulation and transmission form one complete cycle, with Qi internally and the physical body externally, thus mutually completing each other. 

7. 陰陽別論 Yin Yang Bie Lun

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	陰陽別論:	
黃帝問曰：人有四經十二從，何謂。歧伯對曰：四經應四時，十二從應十二月，十二月應十二脈。脈有陰陽，知陽者知陰，知陰者知陽。凡陽有五，五五二十五陽。所謂陰者，真藏也，見則為敗，敗必死也；所謂陽者，胃脘之陽也。別於陽者，知病處也；別於陰者，知死生之期。三陽在頭，三陰在手，所謂一也。別於陽者，知病忌時；別於陰者，知死生之期。謹熟陰陽，無與眾謀。所謂陰陽者，去者為陰，至者為陽；靜者為陰，動者為陽；遲者為陰，數者為陽。凡持真脈之藏脈者，肝至懸絕急，十八日死；心至懸絕，九日死；肺至懸絕，十二日死；腎至懸絕，七日死；脾至懸絕，四日死。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "A person has four meridians and twelve collateral channels. What do they mean?" Qibo replied: "The four meridians correspond to the four seasons, and the twelve collateral channels correspond to the twelve months. The twelve months in turn correspond to the twelve pulses." The pulses have yin and yang; he who knows yang also knows yin, and he who knows yin also knows yang. Generally, there are five types of yang, making twenty-five yangs in total when each is multiplied by five. What is called yin refers to the true zang organs; if they appear, it indicates failure, and failure inevitably leads to death. What is called yang refers to the yang of the stomach region. To distinguish between yin and yang is to know the location of the disease; To differentiate yin is to know the time of life or death. The three yang meridians are located in the head, and the three yin meridians are in the hands; this is what is called unity. To distinguish between yang is to know when a disease should be avoided; To differentiate yin is to understand the time of life and death. Carefully master yin and yang, and there will be no need to consult with others. What is called yin and yang: what departs is yin, what arrives is yang; what remains still is yin, and what moves is yang. What is slow is yin, and what is rapid is yang. Generally, when holding the true pulse of the zang organs, if the liver pulse reaches a state of suspension and tension, death will occur within eighteen days; if the heart pulse reaches a state of suspension, death occurs in nine days; if the lung pulse reaches a state of suspension and is taut, death will occur in twelve days; if the kidney pulse reaches a state of suspension, it results in death within seven days; if the spleen pulse reaches a state of suspension, then death occurs within four days.

2
打開字典
	陰陽別論:	
曰：二陽之病，發心脾，有不得隱曲，女子不月；其傳為風消，其傳為息賁者，死不治。曰：三陽為病，發寒熱，下為癰腫，及為痿厥腨㾓；其傳為索澤，其傳為頹疝。曰：一陽發病，少氣善欬善泄；其傳為心掣，其傳為隔。二陽一陰發病，主驚駭背痛，善噫善欠，名曰風厥。二陰一陽發病，善脹心滿善氣。三陽三陰發病，為偏枯痿易，四支不舉。
		It is said: "Diseases of the two yang meridians originate in the heart and spleen, causing one to be unable to conceal or express emotions; women will experience absence of menstruation." Those who develop wind-xiao as a complication, and those whose condition progresses to xi-ben, are incurable and will die. It is said: "Diseases of the three yang meridians manifest as chills and fever, with abscesses forming below; they may also lead to atrophy, cold extremities, and cramping in the calves." Those who progress to suoze or tui shan will suffer from these conditions. It is said: "Disease of the one yang meridian causes shortness of breath, frequent coughing, and easy diarrhea." Those who progress to xinche or ge will suffer from these conditions. Disease arising from the two yang and one yin meridians mainly causes startlement, back pain, frequent belching, and frequent sighing; it is called wind-jué. Disease arising from two yin and one yang meridians causes a tendency to swell, fullness in the heart region, and excessive qi. Disease arising from three yang and three yin meridians results in hemiplegia or atrophy with weakness, and the four limbs are unable to move.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	陰陽別論:	
鼓一陽曰鉤，鼓一陰曰毛，鼓陽勝急曰絃，鼓陽至而絕曰石，陰陽相過曰溜。陰爭於內，陽擾於外，魄汗未藏，四逆而起，起則熏肺，使人喘鳴。陰之所生，和本曰和。是故剛與剛，陽氣破散，陰氣乃消亡。淖則剛柔不和，經氣乃絕。死陰之屬，不過三日而死；生陽之屬，不過四日而死。所謂生陽死陰者，肝之心，謂之生陽。心之肺，謂之死陰。肺之腎，謂之重陰。腎之脾，謂之辟陰，死不治。結陽者，腫四支。結陰者便血一升，再結二升，三結三升。陰陽結斜，多陰少陽曰石水，少腹腫。二陽結謂之消，三陽結謂之隔，三陰結謂之水，一陰一陽結謂之喉痺。陰搏陽別謂之有子。陰陽虛腸辟死。陽加於陰謂之汗。陰虛陽搏謂之崩。三陰俱搏，二十日夜半死。二陰俱搏，十三日夕時死。一陰俱搏，十日死。三陽俱搏且鼓，三日死。三陰三陽俱搏，心腹滿。發盡不得隱曲，五日死。二陽俱搏，其病溫，死不治，不過十日死。
		A pulse that surges in one yang is called gou; a pulse that surges in one yin is called mao. A pulse where the yang is strong and taut is called xian; a pulse where yang arrives but then ceases abruptly is called shi. When yin and yang alternate, it is called liu. When yin struggles internally and yang disturbs externally, the body fluids have not yet been stored; this causes reversal of the four extremities. When one arises from such a state, it affects the lungs, causing shortness of breath and wheezing. The origin of yin is harmony; when its fundamental nature remains balanced, this is called he (harmony). Therefore, when rigidity meets rigidity, the yang qi disperses and breaks apart, and yin qi then dissipates and perishes. If there is excessive moisture, rigidity and softness will not harmonize, and the jing qi will be cut off. Those belonging to dead yin will die within no more than three days; those belonging to living yang will not live beyond four days. What is called "living yang" and "dead yin"—the liver's connection with the heart is referred to as living yang. The heart's connection with the lungs is referred to as dead yin. The lungs' connection with the kidneys is called double yin. The kidneys' connection with the spleen is called biyin, which is an incurable death condition. Those who suffer from jieyang will experience swelling in all four limbs. Those suffering from jieyin will pass blood with each bowel movement: one sheng the first time, two sheng the second time, and three sheng the third time. When yin and yang become entangled obliquely, with excessive yin and deficient yang, it is called shishui; this results in swelling of the lower abdomen. When two yangs become entangled, it is called xiao; when three yangs are entangled, it is termed ge. When three yins are entangled, it is known as shui. When one yin and one yang become entangled, it is referred to as houbi (laryngeal obstruction). When the yin pulse strikes forcefully and the yang pulse separates distinctly, this is called having a child. Deficiency of both yin and yang leads to intestinal obstruction and death. When yang is added upon yin, it is called sweating. Yin deficiency with strong yang pulse is termed beng (abnormal uterine bleeding). When all three yins pulse strongly, death will occur at midnight on the twentieth day. When two yins both pulse strongly, death occurs in the evening of the thirteenth day. When one yin pulses strongly, death will occur on the tenth day. When all three yangs pulse strongly and surge, death occurs within three days. When both the three yins and three yangs pulse together forcefully, it causes fullness in the heart and abdomen. Symptoms will fully manifest with an inability to conceal or express emotions, leading to death within five days. When two yangs both pulse strongly, the disease is warm in nature; it is incurable and will result in death no later than ten days. 

8. 靈蘭秘典論 Linglan Midian Lun

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	靈蘭秘典... :	
黃帝問曰：願聞十二藏之相使，貴賤何如。歧伯對曰：悉乎哉問也，請遂言之。心者，君主之官也，神明出焉。肺者，相傅之官，治節出焉。肝者，將軍之官，謀慮出焉。膽者，中正之官，決斷出焉。膻中者，臣使之官，喜樂出焉。脾胃者，倉廩之官，五味出焉。大腸者，傳道之官，變化出焉。小腸者，受盛之官，化物出焉。腎者，作強之官，伎巧出焉。三焦者，決瀆之官，水道出焉。膀胱者，州都之官，津液藏焉，氣化則能出矣。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I would like to hear about how the twelve zang organs work together; which is more important and which less? Qibo replied: That is a most thorough question. Please allow me to explain it in detail. The heart is the official of the sovereign ruler; from it arise spirit and wisdom. The lungs are the official of the prime minister, from which arises regulation and coordination. The liver is the general's official; from it arise planning and deliberation. The gallbladder is the official of impartial judgment, from which arises decisiveness. Shanzhong is the official who serves and conveys orders; from it arise joy and happiness. The spleen and stomach are the officials of granaries, from which arise the five flavors. The large intestine is the official responsible for transmission; from it arises transformation. The small intestine is the official in charge of receiving and containing substances, from which arise processed nutrients. The kidneys are the officials responsible for strength; from them arise skills and dexterity. The triple burner is the official in charge of waterways, through which water passages flow. The bladder is the official governing all regions; it stores body fluids and moisture, and when qi transforms them, they can be excreted.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	靈蘭秘典... :	
凡此十二官者，不得相失也。故主明則下安，以此養生則壽，歿世不殆，以為天下則大昌。主不明則十二官危，使道閉塞而不通，形乃大傷，以此養生則殃，以為天下者，其宗大危，戒之戒之。
		All these twelve officials must not lose harmony with one another. Therefore, when the sovereign is wise and discerning, his subordinates will be at peace; cultivating life in this way brings longevity. Even after death, one remains undisturbed, and if such a person serves as an example for the world, great prosperity will follow. If the sovereign is not wise, then the twelve officials are in danger; their functions become blocked and stagnant, resulting in great injury to the body. Cultivating life in this way brings disaster, and if such a person governs the world, his lineage will be greatly endangered. One must take caution, one must take caution.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	靈蘭秘典... :	
至道在微，變化無窮，孰知其原；窘乎哉，消者瞿瞿，孰知其要；閔閔之當，孰者為良。恍惚之數，生於毫氂，毫氂之數，起於度量，千之萬之，可以益大，推之大之，其形乃制。黃帝曰：善哉，余聞精光之道，大聖之業，而宣明大道，非齋戒擇吉日，不敢受也。黃帝乃擇吉日良兆，而藏靈蘭之室，以傳保焉。
		The ultimate principle lies in subtlety; transformations are boundless—who can know their origin? How difficult it is! The wise and the cautious—how many truly understand its essence? Among those who are earnestly seeking, who can be considered excellent? The subtle and indistinct numbers originate from the tiniest hairs; these minute quantities arise from measurements. Multiply them by thousands or ten-thousands, they can increase greatly. Extend and expand them further, and their form is thus determined. The Yellow Emperor said: Excellent! Having heard the doctrine of refined brilliance and the great achievements of a sage, I must publicly declare this grand principle. Without fasting, purification, and choosing an auspicious day, I dare not accept it. The Yellow Emperor therefore selected a propitious day with favorable omens and stored the teachings in the Hall of Linglan, to be preserved and transmitted. 

9. 六節藏象論 Liu Jie Cang Xiang Lun: On the Six Periods and Internal Organ Manifestations

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六節藏象... :	
黃帝問曰：余聞天以六六之節，以成一歲，人以九九制會，計人亦有三百六十五節以為天地久矣，不知其所謂也。歧伯對曰：昭乎哉問也，請遂言之。夫六六之節，九九制會者，所以正天之度、氣之數也。天度者，所以制日月之行也；氣數者，所以紀化生之用也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I have heard that Heaven uses the six-by-six cycle to complete one year, and human beings use the nine-by-nine system of cycles. It is said that humans also have 365 joints corresponding to Heaven and Earth; this has been long established, but I do not know what it means exactly. Qibo replied: What a clear question! Please allow me to explain further. The six-by-six cycle and the nine-by-nine system are used to establish the standard measurements of Heaven's cycles and the numerical patterns of Qi. Heavenly measurement refers to the standard that regulates the movements of the sun and moon; The numerical patterns of Qi are used to record the functions of transformation and generation.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六節藏象... :	
天為陽，地為陰；日為陽，月為陰。行有分紀，周有道理，日行一度，月行十三度而有奇焉，故大小月三百六十五日而成歲積氣餘而盈閏矣。立端於始，表正於中，推餘於終，而天度畢矣。
		Heaven is Yang, and Earth is Yin; The sun is Yang, and the moon is Yin. Their movements have divisions and records, and their cycles follow principles. The sun moves one degree per day, while the moon travels thirteen degrees plus a fraction each day. Therefore, with alternating large and small months totaling 365 days to complete a year, accumulated surplus Qi results in an intercalary month. Establishing the beginning with precision, aligning the center correctly, and calculating the remainder at the end—thus the measurement of Heaven is complete.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六節藏象... :	
帝曰：余已聞天度矣，願聞氣數何以合之。歧伯曰：天以六六為節，地以九九制會，天有十日，日六竟而周甲，甲六復而終歲，三百六十日法也。夫自古通天者，生之本，本於陰陽。其氣九州九竅，皆通乎天氣。故其生五，其氣三，三而成天，三而成地，三而成人，三而三之，合則為九，九分為九野，九野為九藏，故形藏四，神藏五，合為九藏以應之也。
		The Emperor said: I have already heard about Heavenly measurements; I would like to know how the numerical patterns of Qi correspond with them. Qibo said: Heaven uses the six-by-six as a cycle, and Earth employs the nine-by-nine to establish meetings. Heaven has ten days; each day is divided into six periods, completing one cycle of Jia (甲) after six days. After another six cycles of Jia, one full year ends, totaling 360 days according to this method. Since ancient times, those who are connected with Heaven form the fundamental source of life, which originates from Yin and Yang. 𝑙𝑜𝑎𝑑𝑖𝑛𝑔... The Qi of the nine provinces and the nine orifices all connect with Heavenly Qi. Therefore, life is derived from five elements, and Qi consists of three aspects. Three combine to form Heaven, three to form Earth, and three to form humans. By combining the threes thrice, they total nine. These nine are divided into nine regions (nine fields), which correspond to nine internal organs. Thus, there are four physical organs and five spiritual organs, making a total of nine organs that respond accordingly.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六節藏象... :	
帝曰：余已聞六六九九之會也，夫子言積氣盈閏，願聞何謂氣。請夫子發蒙解惑焉。歧伯曰：此上帝所秘，先師傳之也。帝曰：請遂聞之。歧伯曰：五日謂之候，三候謂之氣，六氣謂之時，四時謂之歲，而各從其主治焉。五運相襲，而皆治之，終朞之日，周而復始，時立氣布，如環無端，候亦同法。故曰：不知年之所加，氣之盛衰，虛實之所起，不可以為工矣。
		The Emperor said: I have already heard about the convergence of six-by-six and nine-by-nine. You mentioned accumulated Qi leading to an intercalary month; I would like to hear what you mean by "Qi." Please, Master, enlighten me and dispel my confusion. Qibo said: This is a secret from the Supreme Deity, passed down by previous teachers. The Emperor said: Please proceed and tell me. Qibo said: Five days are called a "hou," three hou make one "Qi," six Qi constitute a season, and four seasons form a year. Each follows its respective governing principle. The Five Periodic Movements succeed one another, each governing in turn. On the final day of a full cycle, they complete and begin anew. Seasons are established, Qi is distributed, like an unbroken ring with no beginning or end; the "hou" also follow this same principle. Therefore it is said: One who does not know which year corresponds to a particular cycle, the waxing and waning of Qi, or the origins of deficiency and excess cannot be considered a skilled practitioner.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六節藏象... :	
帝曰：五運之始，如環無端，其太過不及何如。歧伯曰：五氣更立，各有所勝，盛虛之變，此其常也。帝曰：平氣何如？歧伯曰：無過者也。帝曰：太過不及奈何。歧伯曰：在經有也。帝曰：何謂所勝。歧伯曰：春勝長夏，長夏勝冬，冬勝夏，夏勝秋，秋勝春，所謂得五行時之勝，各以氣命其藏。帝曰：何以知其勝。歧伯曰：求其至也，皆歸始春，未至而至，此謂太過，則薄所不勝，而乘所勝也，命曰氣淫。不分邪僻內生，工不能禁。至而不至，此謂不及，則所勝妄行，而所生受病，所不勝薄之也，命曰氣迫。所謂求其至者，氣至之時也。謹候其時，氣可與期，失時反候，五治不分，邪僻內生，工不能禁也。帝曰：有不襲乎。歧伯曰：蒼天之氣，不得無常也。氣之不襲，是謂非常，非常則變矣。帝曰：非常而變柰何。歧伯曰：變至則病所，勝則微，所不勝則甚，因而重感於邪，則死矣。故非其時則微，當其時則甚也。
		The Emperor said: The beginning of the Five Periodic Movements is like an unbroken ring, with no end or start; what then are the consequences when a movement is excessive or insufficient? Qibo said: The Five Qi successively take their turns, each having something it overcomes. Changes in excess and deficiency are a natural occurrence. The Emperor asked: What about the balanced state of Qi? Qibo said: It is a state without excess. The Emperor asked: What about when Qi is excessive or insufficient? Qibo said: This is recorded in the classical texts. The Emperor asked: What do you mean by "what it overcomes"? Qibo said: Spring overcomes the long summer, the long summer overcomes winter, winter overcomes summer, summer overcomes autumn, and autumn overcomes spring. This is what is meant by the Five Elements' seasonal dominance; each season's Qi corresponds to a specific internal organ. The Emperor asked: How can one know which element overcomes another? Qibo said: Determine this by observing their arrival. All return to the beginning of spring. If an element arrives before its time, it is excessive; in such cases, it will weaken what it does not overcome and overpower what it should overcome. This condition is called "Qi Yin" (excessive Qi). If one cannot distinguish between external pathogenic influences or internal imbalances, a practitioner will be unable to prevent them. If an element is due but does not arrive, this is termed insufficiency. In such cases, the element it should overcome will act recklessly, and its offspring will suffer illness; meanwhile, what it cannot overcome will be weakened. This condition is called "Qi Po" (constrained Qi). What is meant by seeking the time of arrival is to determine when a particular Qi should appear. One must carefully observe the time; then, Qi can be anticipated and predicted. If one misses the timing or misjudges the signs, the five governing principles become indistinct, leading to internal imbalances and pathogenic influences that a practitioner cannot prevent. The Emperor asked: Are there cases where this pattern is not followed? Qibo said: The Qi of the Azure Heaven cannot be without regularity. 𝑙𝑜𝑎𝑑𝑖𝑛𝑔... If a Qi does not follow its usual pattern, this is called an abnormal condition; when something is abnormal, it leads to change. The Emperor asked: What happens when there are changes due to these abnormalities? Qibo said: When a change occurs, it causes illness. If the affected element is what should be overcome (the "so-sheng"), the condition will be mild; if it affects what cannot be overcome ("bu-sheng"), the condition will be severe. If this is compounded by further exposure to pathogenic influences, death may occur. Therefore, when an illness occurs outside its corresponding season, it tends to be mild; but if it occurs during the appropriate season, it becomes severe.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六節藏象... :	
帝曰：善。余聞氣合而有形，因變以正名。天地之運，陰陽之化，其於萬物，孰少孰多，可得聞乎。歧伯曰：悉哉問也，天至廣不可度，地至大不可量，大神靈問，請陳其方。草生五色，五色之變，不可勝視；草生五味，五味之美，不可勝極，嗜欲不同，各有所通。天食人以五氣，地食人以五味。五氣入鼻，藏於心肺，上使五色脩明，音聲能彰。五味入口，藏於腸胃，味有所藏，以養五氣，氣和而生津液相成，神乃自生。
		The Emperor said: Well said. I have heard that when Qi combines, form is generated; and names are established according to the changes they undergo. The movements of Heaven and Earth, the transformations of Yin and Yang—regarding all things in existence, which are few and which are many? May I hear your explanation. Qibo said: What a thorough question! Heaven is so vast it cannot be measured, and Earth is so great it cannot be quantified. Your divine inquiry deserves an answer; please allow me to present the principles. Grasses produce five colors, and the transformations of these five colors are too numerous to behold; Plants give rise to five flavors, and the richness of these five flavors is beyond measure. Preferences vary among individuals, each connecting with something different. Heaven nourishes humans through Five Qi, and Earth nourishes them through the Five Flavors. The Five Qi enter through the nose, are stored in the heart and lungs, and above, they enable clear complexion and distinct voice. The Five Flavors enter through the mouth, are stored in the intestines and stomach. These flavors are retained to nourish the Five Qi; when Qi is harmonized, body fluids are generated, and together they form a balanced state, allowing spirit (Shen) to arise naturally.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六節藏象... :	
帝曰：藏象何如。歧伯曰：心者，生之本，神之變也，其華在面，其充在血脈，為陽中之太陽，通於夏氣。肺者，氣之本，魄之處也，其華在毛，其充在皮，為陽中之太陰，通於秋氣。腎者，主蟄封藏之本，精之處也，其華在髮，其充在骨，為陰中之少陰，通於冬氣。肝者，罷極之本，魂之居也，其華在爪，其充在筋，以生血氣，其味酸，其色蒼，此為陽中之少陽，通於春氣。脾胃大腸小腸三焦膀胱者，倉廩之本，營之居也，名曰器，能化糟粕，轉味而入出者也，其華在脣四白，其充在肌，其味甘，其色黃，此至陰之類通於土氣。凡十一藏取決於膽也。
		The Emperor asked: What is the nature of the internal organs? Qibo said: The heart is the fundamental source of life and the organ where spirit (Shen) manifests. Its brilliance appears on the face, its fullness resides in the blood vessels; it belongs to Yang among Yangers, corresponding with summer Qi. The lungs are the fundamental source of Qi and the dwelling place of Po (corporeal soul). Their brilliance appears in the hair, their fullness resides in the skin; they belong to Yin among Yangs, corresponding with autumn Qi. The kidneys are the root of concealment and storage, the dwelling place of essence (Jing). Their brilliance appears in hair, their fullness resides within bones; they belong to Yin within Yins, corresponding with winter Qi. The liver is the root of exhaustion and recovery, the dwelling place of Hun (ethereal soul). Its brilliance appears in nails, its fullness resides within tendons; it generates blood and Qi. Its flavor is sour, its color is greenish-blue; this organ belongs to Yang within Yangers, corresponding with spring Qi. The spleen, stomach, large intestine, small intestine, triple burner, and bladder are the roots of storage, where nutrition resides. They are collectively called "Qi" (vessels), capable of transforming waste matter and processing flavors for excretion and absorption. Their brilliance appears in the lips and the four white areas around them; their fullness is found in muscles; their flavor is sweet, their color yellow—these belong to the most Yin category, corresponding with Earth Qi. All eleven internal organs depend on the gallbladder for decision-making.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六節藏象... :	
故人迎一盛病在少陽，二盛病在太陽，三盛病在陽明，四盛已上為格陽。寸口一盛，病在厥陰，二盛病在少陰，三盛病在太陰，四盛已上為關陰。人迎與寸口俱盛四倍已上為關格，關格之脈贏，不能極於天地之精氣，則死矣。
		Therefore, if the Renying pulse is one degree stronger than normal, illness resides in Shaoyang; two degrees stronger indicates disease in Taiyang; three degrees stronger suggests illness in Yangming. If it becomes four or more times stronger, this condition is termed "Ge Yang." If the Cunkou pulse is one degree stronger, illness resides in Jueyin; two degrees stronger indicates disease within Shaoyin; three times stronger suggests illness within Taiyin. If it becomes four or more degrees stronger, this condition is called "Guan Yin." If both the Renying and Cunkou pulses are four times stronger than normal, this condition is termed "Guan Ge." When a pulse of Guan Ge appears weak, it indicates that one cannot fully receive the refined Qi from Heaven and Earth—this leads to death. 

10. 五藏生成 Generation of the Five Zang Organs

1
打開字典
	五藏生成:	
心之合脈也，其榮色也，其主腎也。肺之合皮也，其榮毛也，其主心也。肝之合筋也，其榮爪也，其主肺也。脾之合肉也，其榮脣也，其主肝也。腎之合骨也，其榮髮也，其主脾也。
		The heart is harmonized with the pericardium; its brilliance appears in color; it governs the kidney. The lung is harmonized with the skin; its brilliance appears in hair; it governs the heart. The liver is harmonized with tendons; its brilliance appears in nails; it governs the lung. The spleen is harmonized with flesh; its brilliance appears on lips; it governs the liver. The kidney is harmonized with bones; its brilliance appears in hair (head hair); it governs the spleen.

2
打開字典
	五藏生成:	
是故多食鹹則脈凝泣而變色；多食苦則皮槁而毛拔；多食辛則筋急而爪枯；多食酸則肉胝䐢而脣揭；多食甘則骨痛而髮落，此五味之所傷也。故心欲苦，肺欲辛，肝欲酸，脾欲甘，腎欲鹹，此五味之所合也。五藏之氣，故色見青如草茲者死，黃如枳實者死，黑如尚炱者死，赤如衃血者死，白如枯骨者死，此五色之見死也。青如翠羽者生，赤如雞冠者生，黃如蟹腹者生，白如豕膏者生，黑如烏羽者生，此五色之見生也。生於心，如以縞裹朱；生於肺，如以縞裹紅；生於肝，如以縞裹紺；生於脾，如以縞裹栝樓實，生於腎，如以縞裹紫，此五藏所生之外榮也。色味當五藏：白當肺，辛，赤當心，苦，青當肝，酸，黃當脾，甘，黑當腎，鹹，故白當皮，赤當脈，青當筋，黃當肉，黑當骨。
		Therefore, excessive consumption of salty flavors causes the vessels to congeal and tears to flow while changing color; excessive consumption of bitter flavors causes the skin to wither and hair to fall out; excessive consumption of pungent flavors causes tendons to become tense and nails to dry up; excessive consumption of sour flavors causes flesh to thicken and lips to crack; excessive consumption of sweet flavors causes bones to ache and hair (head hair) to fall out. These are the injuries caused by the five flavors. Therefore, the heart desires bitter flavor, the lung desires pungent flavor, the liver desires sour flavor, the spleen desires sweet flavor, and the kidney desires salty flavor. These are the harmonies of the five flavors. The five zang organs' qi, therefore when the color appears blue like newly sprouted grass, yellow like citron fruit, black like lamp soot, red like clotted blood, or white like dry bones, it indicates death. These are the five colors that indicate death. Blue like emerald feathers indicates life, red like a rooster's comb indicates life, yellow like a crab's belly indicates life, white like pork fat indicates life, black like crow feathers indicates life. These are the five colors indicating life. When born from the heart, it appears as if red is wrapped in white silk; when born from the lung, it appears as if crimson is wrapped in white silk; when born from the liver, it appears as if indigo is wrapped in white silk; when born from the spleen, it appears as if Trichosanthes fruit is wrapped in white silk; when born from the kidney, it appears as if purple is wrapped in white silk. These are the external manifestations generated by the five zang organs. Colors and flavors correspond to the five zang organs: white corresponds to the lung, pungent; red corresponds to the heart, bitter; blue corresponds to the liver, sour; yellow corresponds to the spleen, sweet; black corresponds to the kidney, salty. Therefore, white corresponds to skin, red corresponds to vessels, blue corresponds to tendons, yellow corresponds to flesh, and black corresponds to bones.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五藏生成:	
諸脈者皆屬於目，諸髓者皆屬於腦，諸筋者皆屬於節，諸血者皆屬於心，諸氣者皆屬於肺，此四支八谿之朝夕也。故人臥，血歸於肝，肝受血而能視，足受血而能步，掌受血而能握，指受血而能攝。臥出而風吹之，血凝於膚者為痺，凝於脈者為泣，凝於足者為厥。此三者，血行而不得反其空，故為痺厥也。人有大谷十二分，小谿三百五十四名，少十二俞，此皆衛氣之所留止，邪氣之所客也，鍼石緣而去之。
		All vessels are connected to the eyes; all marrow is connected to the brain; all tendons are connected to joints; all blood is connected to the heart; all qi is connected to the lungs. These are the daily rhythms of the four limbs and eight streams. Therefore, when a person lies down, blood returns to the liver; the liver receives blood and thus enables vision; the feet receive blood and thus enable walking; the palms receive blood and thus enable grasping; the fingers receive blood and thus enable holding. If a person is lying down and exposed to wind, blood congealing on the skin causes bi syndrome; blood congealing in the vessels causes tears; blood congealing at the feet causes jue syndrome. These three conditions occur because blood flows but cannot return to its original space, hence they are called bi and jue syndromes. The human body has twelve major valleys, 354 minor streams with names, and twelve fewer yu points; these are all places where defensive qi resides and where pathogenic factors reside. Acupuncture and moxibustion follow the meridians to remove them.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五藏生成:	
診病之始五決為紀，欲知其始，先建其母，所謂五決者五脈也。是以頭痛巔疾，下虛上實過在足少陰，巨陽甚則入腎。徇蒙招尤目冥耳聾，下實上虛，過在足少陽，厥陰甚則入肝。腹滿䐜脹，支鬲胠脇，下厥上冒，過在足太陰，陽明。欬嗽上氣，厥在胸中，過在手陽明太陰。心煩頭痛病在鬲中，過在手巨陽，少陰。
		At the beginning of diagnosing a disease, the Five Decisions serve as guidelines. To understand its origin, one must first establish its root. The so-called "Five Decisions" refer to the five pulses. Therefore, headache and vertex disease result from deficiency below and excess above; the cause lies in the foot Shaoyin. If the Taiyang is excessive, it may penetrate into the kidney. Confusion of consciousness, visual obscurity, and deafness result from excess below and deficiency above; the cause lies in foot Shaoyang. If Jueyin is excessive, it may penetrate into liver. Abdominal fullness and distension, with obstruction of the chest and flanks, coldness below and dizziness above, result from involvement in foot Taiyin and Yangming. Coughing with upward rushing qi, reversal of qi in the chest, results from involvement in hand Yangming and Taiyin. Palpitation, headache, with disease located between the diaphragm, results from involvement of hand Taiyang and Shaoyin.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五藏生成:	
夫脈之小大滑濇浮沈，可以指別；五藏之象，可以類推；五藏相音，可以意識；五色微診，可以目察。能合脈色，可以萬全。赤脈之至也喘而堅，診曰有積氣在中，時害於食，名曰心痺，得之外疾，思慮而心虛，故邪從之。白脈之至也喘而浮，上虛下實。驚，有積氣在胸中，喘而虛名曰肺痺寒熱，得之醉而使內也。青脈之至也長而左右彈有積氣在心下支胠，名曰肝痺，得之寒濕，與疝同法，腰痛足清頭痛。黃脈之至也大而虛，有積氣在腹中，有厥氣名曰厥疝，女子同法，得之疾使四支汗出當風。黑脈之至也上堅而大有積氣在小腹與陰，名曰腎痺，得之沐浴清水而臥。
		The size, smoothness or roughness, floating or sinking nature of pulses can be distinguished by touch; the appearances of the five zang organs can be analogically inferred; the mutual relationships between the five zang and their corresponding sounds can be understood through awareness; subtle observations of the five colors can be discerned with the eyes. If one is able to integrate pulse diagnosis and color observation, perfect accuracy in diagnosis can be achieved. When red pulses arrive, they are labored and firm; diagnosis indicates accumulated qi in the middle jiao, which at times harms digestion. This condition is called heart bi syndrome. It arises from external disease, excessive thinking leading to a vacuity of the heart, hence pathogenic factors take advantage of it. When white pulses arrive, they are labored yet floating; this indicates deficiency above and excess below. There is a startle response, with accumulated qi in the chest; labored breathing and vacuity are present. This condition is named lung bi syndrome with cold-heat symptoms. It results from excessive drinking followed by internal exertion. When green pulses arrive, they are long and rebound on both sides; there is accumulated qi beneath the heart affecting the flanks. This condition is called liver bi syndrome. It results from cold-dampness, sharing a similar mechanism with hernia, presenting symptoms of lumbago, cold feet, and headache. When yellow pulses arrive, they are large yet vacuous; there is accumulated qi in the abdomen and reversal of qi. This condition is called jue shan (a type of hernia). Women exhibit similar symptoms. It results from overexertion causing perspiration in all four limbs while being exposed to wind. When black pulses arrive, they are firm and large; there is accumulated qi in the lower abdomen and genital area. This condition is called kidney bi syndrome. It results from bathing in cold water and then lying down.

	五藏生成:	
凡相五色之奇脈，面黃目青，面黃目赤，面黃目白，面黃目黑者，皆不死也。面青目赤，面赤目白，面青目黑，面黑目白，面赤目青，皆死也。
