Those with a blue face and red eyes, red face and white eyes, blue face and black eyes, black face and white eyes, or red face and blue eyes will all die. 

11. 五藏別論 Discourse on the Five Zang Organs

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五藏別論:	
黃帝問曰：余聞方士，或以腦髓為藏，或以腸胃為藏，或以為府，敢問更相反皆自謂是不知其道，願聞其說。歧伯對曰：腦髓骨脈膽女子胞，此六者地氣之所生也，皆藏於陰而象於地，故藏而不寫，名曰奇恆之府。夫胃大腸小腸三焦膀胱，此五者，天氣之所生也，其氣象天故寫而不藏，此受五藏濁氣，名曰傳化之府，此不能久留輸寫者也。魄門亦為五藏，使水穀不得久藏。所謂五藏者，藏精氣而不寫也，故滿而不能實。六府者，傳化物而不藏，故實而不能滿也。所以然者，水穀入口，則胃實而腸虛；食下，則腸實而胃虛。故曰：實而不滿，滿而不實也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I have heard among the Fangshi, some regard brain and marrow as zang (viscera), others consider intestines and stomachs as zang or fu (hollow organs). I dare to ask why these views are opposite yet each claims its own correctness. I do not know their principles; I wish to hear your explanation. Qibo replied: Brain, marrow, bones, vessels, gallbladder, and the uterus in women—these six are produced by the earth qi. They all reside within yin and correspond to the earth; therefore, they store but do not discharge. These are called the "Qi Heng Fu" (extraordinary fu organs). The stomach, large intestine, small intestine, sanjiao (triple burner), and bladder—these five are produced by the heaven qi. Their functions resemble that of heaven; therefore, they discharge but do not store. These receive the turbid qi from the five zang organs and are called "Chuan Hua Fu" (transmission and transformation fu organs). They cannot retain substances for long and must transport and discharge them. The 魄门 (posterior orifice) also functions as part of the five zang, ensuring that water and grains do not remain stored for long. What is referred to as the five zang organs are those that store essence and qi without discharging; therefore, they are full but not solid. The six fu organs transmit and transform substances without storing them; thus, they are filled with content but cannot be completely full. This is because when water and grains enter the mouth, the stomach becomes filled while the intestines become empty; after food has been consumed, the intestines become filled while the stomach becomes empty. Therefore it is said: "Filled but not full, and full but not filled."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五藏別論:	
帝曰：氣口何以獨為五藏主。歧伯曰：胃者水穀之海，六府之大源也。五味入口，藏於胃以養五藏氣，氣口亦太陰也。是以五藏六府之氣味，皆出於胃，變見於氣口。故五氣入鼻，藏於心肺，心肺有病而鼻為之不利也。
		The Emperor asked: Why is the qi kou (radial artery at the wrist) uniquely regarded as the master of the five zang? Qibo said: The stomach is the sea of water and grains, the great source of the six fu organs. The five flavors enter the mouth and are stored in the stomach to nourish the qi of the five zang. The qi kou also belongs to Taiyin. Therefore, the flavors and qi of the five zang and six fu organs all originate from the stomach and are manifested at the qi kou. Hence, the five types of qi enter through the nose and are stored in the heart and lungs; when the heart or lungs have disease, the nose becomes affected.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五藏別論:	
凡治病必察其下，適其脈，觀其志意，與其病也。拘於鬼神者，不可與言至德。惡於鍼石者，不可與言至巧。病不許治者病必不治，治之無功矣。
		Whenever treating a disease, one must examine its lower aspect, adjust to the pulse, observe the patient's will and intention, and understand their condition. Those who are bound by beliefs in ghosts and spirits cannot be taught about the highest virtue. Those who dislike acupuncture and moxibustion cannot be spoken to about supreme skill. If a patient refuses treatment, the illness will certainly not be cured; treating it would achieve nothing. 

12. 異法方宜論 Yi Fa Fang Yi Lun

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	異法方宜... :	
黃帝問曰：醫之治病也，一病而治各不同皆愈何也。歧伯對曰：地勢使然也。故東方之域，天地之所始生也，魚鹽之地，海濱傍水，其民食魚而嗜鹹，皆安其處，美其食，魚者使人熱中，鹽者勝血，故其民皆黑色踈理，其病皆為癰瘍，其治宜砭石，故砭石者，亦從東方來。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: When physicians treat illnesses, they all cure one illness with different treatments; why is this so? Qibo replied: This is due to the terrain. Therefore, in the eastern region, where heaven and earth first gave birth to life, it is a land of fish and salt, situated by the seashore with water nearby. The people there eat fish and have a taste for salt; they are content with their environment and enjoy their food. Fish causes internal heat, while salt overcomes blood, so the people there tend to have dark complexions and loose skin textures. Their illnesses often manifest as abscesses, and acupuncture stones (bian shi) are most suitable for treating them. Hence, the use of acupuncture stones originated from the eastern region.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	異法方宜... :	
西方者，金玉之域，沙石之處，天地之所收引也，其民陵居而多風，水土剛強，其民不衣而褐薦，其民華食而脂肥，故邪不能傷其形體，其病生於內，其治宜毒藥，故毒藥者，亦從西方來。
		The western region is a land of metal and jade, with sandy and stony terrain; it is where heaven and earth converge and draw in energy. The people there live on elevated ground and are often exposed to wind, and the water and soil are hard and strong. These people do not wear fine clothes but instead wear coarse hemp garments and eat simple food, yet they consume rich and fatty diets. Therefore, external pathogens cannot easily harm their bodies; their illnesses arise internally, and thus internal prescriptions (toxic herbs) are most appropriate for treatment. Hence, the use of toxic herbs originated from the western region.

3
打開字典
	異法方宜... :	
北方者，天地所閉藏之域也，其地高陵居，風寒冰冽，其民樂野處而乳食，藏寒生滿病，其治宜灸焫，故灸焫者，亦從北方來。
		The northern region is a domain where heaven and earth close in and store energy. The land there is high and elevated, with cold winds and icy conditions. The people enjoy living outdoors and consume dairy products; internal cold accumulates, leading to fullness-type illnesses. For such ailments, moxibustion (灸焫) is most suitable as a treatment. Hence, the practice of moxibustion originated from the northern region.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	異法方宜... :	
南方者，天地所長養，陽之所盛處也，其地下，水土弱，霧露之所聚也，其民嗜酸而食胕，故其民皆緻理而赤色，其病攣痺，其治宜微鍼，故九鍼者，亦從南方來。
		The southern region is where heaven and earth nurture life, a place of abundant yang energy. The land there lies low, with weak water and soil, and it gathers mist and dew. The people have a preference for sour flavors and consume spoiled food; thus, they tend to have fine skin textures and red complexions. Their illnesses are often characterized by cramps and arthralgia (bi syndrome), and the appropriate treatment is acupuncture using fine needles. Hence, the nine types of acupuncture needles originated from the southern region.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	異法方宜... :	
中央者，其地平以濕，天地所以生萬物也眾，其民食雜而不勞，故其病多痿厥寒熱，其治宜導引按蹻，故導引按蹻者，亦從中央出也。故聖人雜合以治，各得其所宜，故治所以異而病皆愈者，得病之情，知治之大體也。
		The central region has level and moist land, where heaven and earth produce a multitude of living things. The people there consume varied diets without excessive labor; thus, their illnesses often include atrophy (wei), coldness or heat in the extremities (jue), and alternating chills and fever. For these conditions, the appropriate treatment is guiding and leading exercises combined with pressing and manipulating techniques (dao yin an qiao). Hence, the practice of guiding and leading exercises along with pressing and manipulating originated from the central region. Therefore, a sage physician combines various methods of treatment, each tailored to what is appropriate. Hence, although treatments differ, all illnesses are cured—this is because the nature of the illness is understood and the general principles of treatment are known. 

13. 移精變氣論 Shifting Spirit and Transforming Vital Energy Lun

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	移精變氣... :	
黃帝問曰：余聞古之治病，惟其移精變氣，可祝由而已。今世治病，毒藥治其內，鍼石治其外，或愈或不愈，何也。歧伯對曰：往古人居禽獸之閒，動作以避寒，陰居以避暑，內無眷慕之累，外無伸官之形，此恬憺之世，邪不能深入也。故毒藥不能治其內，鍼石不能治其外，故可移精祝由而已。當今之世不然，憂患緣其內，苦形傷其外，又失四時之從，逆寒暑之宜，賊風數至，虛邪朝夕，內至五藏骨髓，外傷空竅肌膚，所以小病必甚，大病必死，故祝由不能已也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I have heard that in ancient times, treating diseases required merely shifting the spirit and transforming vital energy; it could be accomplished simply by reciting incantations. In modern times, treating diseases involves using toxic medicines internally and acupuncture or stones externally; yet some are cured while others are not. Why is this so? Qibo replied: In ancient times, people lived among birds and beasts. They moved about to avoid the cold and remained in shady places to avoid summer heat. Internally, they had no burdens of longing or attachment; externally, there was no form of contention for official positions. This was a peaceful age, and pathogenic influences could not penetrate deeply. Therefore, toxic medicines were ineffective for internal ailments, and acupuncture or stones were ineffective for external conditions; thus, it was sufficient to merely shift the spirit and recite incantations. In the present age, it is different. Worry and distress arise internally, while toil and hardship injure externally. Moreover, people lose harmony with the four seasons and act contrary to what is appropriate for cold or heat. Harmful winds frequently arrive; pathogenic influences come at dawn and dusk. Internally, they reach the five zang organs and bone marrow; externally, they damage openings, pores, and skin. Therefore, minor illnesses inevitably become severe, and major illnesses inevitably lead to death—thus, reciting incantations alone cannot cure them.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	移精變氣... :	
帝曰：善。余欲臨病人，觀死生，決嫌疑，欲知其要，如日月光，可得聞乎。歧伯曰：色脈者，上帝之所貴也，先師之所傳也。上古使僦貸季，理色脈而通神明，合之金木水火土四時八風六合，不離其常，變化相移，以觀其妙，以知其要，欲知其要，則色脈是矣。色以應日，脈以應月，常求其要，則其要也。夫色之變化，以應四時之脈，此上帝之所貴，以合於神明也，所以遠死而近生。生道以長，命曰聖王。中古之治病，至而治之，湯液十日，以去八風五痺之病，十日不已，治以草蘇草荄之枝，本末為助，標本已得，邪氣乃服。暮世之治病也則不然，治不本四時，不知日月，不審逆從，病形已成，乃欲微鍼治其外，湯液治其內，粗工兇兇，以為可攻，故病未已，新病復起。
		The Emperor said: Well said. I wish to examine patients, observe life and death, resolve doubts and uncertainties, and understand the essentials as clearly as the light of the sun and moon. May I hear about this? lords Qibo said: Color and pulse are what the Supreme Deity values, and they are what our predecessors have transmitted. In ancient times, the sage Juodai Ji analyzed color and pulse to communicate with divine wisdom. He harmonized these with the Five Elements—metal, wood, water, fire, earth—the four seasons, eight winds, and 六合 (the six directions), never departing from their normal patterns. Through understanding transformations and mutual influences, he observed their subtleties and grasped their essentials. If one wishes to know the essentials, then color and pulse are indeed key. Color corresponds to the sun, and pulse corresponds to the moon. Continuously seeking their essentials will reveal those very essentials. The changes in color correspond to the pulses of the four seasons; this is what the Supreme Deity esteems, as it harmonizes with divine wisdom. Therefore, it enables one to avoid death and approach life. Those who follow the path of life and live long are called sage kings. In the middle ancient times, when treating diseases, treatment was initiated upon onset. Decoctions were used for ten days to eliminate ailments caused by the eight winds and five arthralgias. If symptoms did not subside after ten days, treatment continued with branches of herbs such as caosu (草蘇) and caogai (草荄), using both roots and leaves as assistants. Once the root and branch of the condition were understood, pathogenic influences would be subdued. In later times, treating diseases was not like this. Treatment did not follow the four seasons, ignored the sun and moon, and failed to discern proper or improper conditions. By the time treatment began, the disease had already taken form; then they wished merely to use acupuncture for external symptoms and decoctions for internal ones. Unskilled physicians, in great haste, thought it possible to attack the illness forcefully. Thus, before the original illness was cured, new ailments arose again.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	移精變氣... :	
帝曰：願聞要道。歧伯曰：治之要極，無失色脈，用之不惑，治之大則。逆從到行，標本不得，亡神失國。去故就新，乃得真人。帝曰：余聞其要於夫子矣，夫子言不離色脈，此余之所知也。歧伯曰：治之極於一。帝曰：何謂一。歧伯曰：一者，因得之。帝曰：奈何。歧伯曰：閉戶塞牖，繫之病者，數問其情，以從其意，得神者昌，失神者亡。帝曰：善。
		The Emperor said: I wish to hear about the essential principles. Qibo said: The most important principle in treatment is not to lose sight of color and pulse; using this knowledge without confusion is the fundamental rule of healing. If one fails to discern proper or improper conditions, and cannot grasp root and branch, then vital spirit will be lost and the state will fall into disorder. By discarding the old and embracing the new, one attains the True Person (zhenren). The Emperor said: I have heard from you, Master, about the essentials. You said that one cannot depart from color and pulse—this is what I already know. Qibo said: The ultimate principle of treatment lies in unity. The Emperor asked: What is meant by "unity"? Qibo said: The term "one" refers to attaining it through proper causes. The Emperor asked: How so? Qibo said: Close the doors and windows, confine the patient, frequently inquire about their condition, and follow their inclinations. Those who preserve their vital spirit will flourish; those who lose it will perish. The Emperor said: Well said. 

14. 湯液醪醴論 Tangye Laoli Lun

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	湯液醪醴... :	
黃帝問曰：為五穀湯液及醪醴，柰何。歧伯對曰：必以稻米，炊之稻薪，稻米者完，稻薪者堅。帝曰：何以然。歧伯曰：此得天地之和，高下之宜，故能至完，伐取得時，故能至堅也。帝曰：上古聖人作湯液醪醴，為而不用，何也。歧伯曰：自古聖人之作湯液醪醴者，以為備耳，夫上古作湯液，故為而弗服也。中古之世，道德稍衰，邪氣時至，服之萬全。帝曰：今之世不必已何也。歧伯曰：當今之世，必齊毒藥攻其中，鑱石鍼艾治其外也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: How should one prepare medicinal decoctions from five grains and fermented grain beverages? Qibo replied: One must use rice, steamed with rice straw. Rice represents completeness, and rice straw represents firmness. The Emperor asked: Why is that so? Qibo said: This is because rice attains the harmony of heaven and earth, and suits both high and low conditions; therefore it can reach completeness. Rice straw is harvested at the proper time, so it can attain firmness. The Emperor asked: The sage men of ancient times made medicinal decoctions and fermented grain beverages, yet prepared them without using them. Why was that? Qibo said: Since ancient times, the sage men made medicinal decoctions and fermented beverages merely as a preparation. In antiquity, when they prepared medicinal decoctions, it was done for readiness, hence they were made but not consumed. In the middle ancient times, morality and virtue had somewhat declined; pathogenic influences occasionally arose, so taking them ensured complete safety. The Emperor asked: Why is it not necessary in today's age? Qibo said: In the present age, one must use potent medicinal substances to attack from within and acupuncture stones, needles, and moxa to treat externally.

2
打開字典
	湯液醪醴... :	
帝曰：形弊血盡而功不立者何。歧伯曰：神不使也。帝曰：何謂神不使。歧伯曰：鍼石道也。精神不進，志意不治，故病不可愈。今精壞神去，榮衛不可復收。何者，嗜欲無窮，而憂患不止，精氣弛壞，榮泣衛除，故神去之而病不愈也。
		The Emperor asked: Why is it that when the body is exhausted and blood depleted, yet no therapeutic effect is achieved? Qibo said: It is because the vital energy does not respond. The Emperor asked: What do you mean by "the vital energy does not respond"? Qibo said: Acupuncture and stones are the methods. If one's spirit does not advance, and their will is not in order, then illness cannot be cured. Now that the essence has been damaged and the vital energy has departed, the functions of nourishing and defending qi can no longer be restored. Why is this so? Because desires are endless and worries cease not, the essence and qi become lax and damaged; nourishing fluids dry up and defending functions dissipate. Therefore, the vital energy departs, and illness cannot be cured.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	湯液醪醴... :	
帝曰：夫病之始生也，極微極精，必先入結於皮膚。今良工皆稱曰：病成名曰逆，則鍼石不能治，良藥不能及也。今良工皆得其法，守其數，親戚兄弟遠近音聲日聞於耳，五色日見於目，而病不愈者，亦何暇不早乎。歧伯曰：病為本，工為標，標本不得，邪氣不服，此之謂也。
		The Emperor said: When a disease first arises, it is extremely subtle and refined; it must initially enter and settle in the skin. Now skilled physicians all say: When a disease has taken form and is called "reversal," then acupuncture stones cannot treat it, and even the best medicines are ineffective. Now skilled physicians have all mastered the methods and adhere to their principles. They hear the voices of relatives and siblings, near or far, daily in their ears; they see the five colors daily with their eyes. Yet if illness still does not subside, is it not because treatment was delayed too long? Qibo said: The disease is the root; the physician is the branch. If the relationship between root and branch is not properly managed, pathogenic influences will not be subdued—this is what it means.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	湯液醪醴... :	
帝曰：其有不從毫毛而生，五藏陽以竭也，津液充郭，其魄獨居，孤精於內，氣耗於外，形不可與衣相保，此四極急而動中，是氣拒於內，而形施於外，治之柰何。歧伯曰：平治於權衡，去宛陳莝，微動四極，溫衣，繆刺其處，以復其形。開鬼門，潔淨府，精以時服，五陽已布，踈滌五藏，故精自生，形自盛，骨肉相保，巨氣乃平。帝曰：善。
		The Emperor asked: There are cases where illness does not arise from the hair and skin, but rather due to exhaustion of the five zang organs' yang. In such cases, body fluids fill the hollow spaces; only the hun spirit remains isolated within, while qi is depleted externally. The physical form cannot maintain itself even with clothing. When the four extremities are tense and internal functions disturbed, this indicates that pathogenic influences have been resisted internally but manifest outwardly in the form. How should such a condition be treated? Qibo said: Treat it by restoring balance as measured on the scale; remove obstructions and accumulated waste. Gently stimulate the four extremities, keep clothing warm, and apply acupuncture at appropriate points to restore the body's form. Open the "ghost gate" to promote perspiration, purify the internal organs, and ensure essence is replenished at appropriate times. When yang energy has spread properly, cleanse and clear the five zang organs; thus, essence will naturally regenerate, the body form will flourish, bones and flesh will be preserved together, and vital qi will return to harmony. The Emperor said: Well said. 

15. 玉版論要 Essential Discourse on the Jade Tablet

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	玉版論要:	
黃帝問曰：余聞揆度奇恒，所指不同，用之柰何。歧伯對曰：揆度者，度病之淺深也。奇恒者，言奇病也。請言道之至數，五色脈變，揆度奇恒，道在於一。神轉不回，回則不轉，乃失其機，至數之要，迫近以微，著之玉版，命曰合玉機。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I have heard that the principles of Kui Du and Qi Heng refer to different things; how should they be used? Qibo replied: Kuidu refers to assessing the depth of a disease. Qi Heng refers to discussing unusual or rare diseases. Please explain the most fundamental principles: the five colors, pulse changes, Kui Du and Qi Heng—these all derive from a single principle. The vital energy moves forward without turning back; if it turns back, then movement ceases and the opportunity is lost. The most essential principles are subtle yet imminent. Record them on jade tablets, and call this "He Yu Ji."

2
打開字典
	玉版論要:	
容色見上下左右，各在其要。其色見淺者，湯液主治，十日已。其見深者，必齊主治，二十一日己。其見大深者，醪酒主治，百日已。色夭面脫，不治，百日盡已。脈短氣絕死，病溫虛甚死。色見上下左右，各在其要。上為逆，下為從。女子右為逆，左為從；男子左為逆，右為從。易重陽死，重陰死。
		The appearance of color can be observed in the upper, lower, left, and right parts of the body; each has its key location. When the color appears faintly, it is primarily treated with decoctions; recovery can be expected within ten days. When the color appears deeply, treatment must involve dietary regulation (Qi); recovery may take twenty-one days. When the color is very deep, it should be primarily treated with medicinal wine; recovery can occur within a hundred days. If the complexion appears dull and the face shows signs of wasting, no treatment is effective; death may occur within a hundred days. A short pulse with breath failure indicates death; illness due to excessive internal cold also leads to death. The appearance of color in the upper, lower, and left-right parts of the body each has its key significance. Color appearing above is considered adverse (ni), while that below is favorable (cong). For women, color on the right side is considered adverse, and on the left side it is favorable. For men, color on the left side is considered adverse, while that on the right side is favorable. Excessive Yang or excessive Yin leads to death.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	玉版論要:	
陰陽反他，治在權衡相奪，奇恒事也，揆度事也。搏脈痺躄，寒熱之交。脈孤為消氣，虛泄為奪血。孤為逆，虛為從。行奇恒之法，以太陰始。行所不勝曰逆，逆則死；行所勝曰從，從則活。八風四時之勝，終而復始，逆行一過，不復可數，論要畢矣。
		When Yin and Yang are reversed, treatment depends on balancing their opposing forces; this is a matter of Qi Heng and Kui Du. A taut pulse indicates arthralgia and lameness, which occur at the intersection of cold and heat conditions. An isolated pulse signifies the dissipation of Qi; deficiency with diarrhea indicates loss of blood. Isolation is considered adverse, while emptiness (deficiency) is favorable. To apply the principles of Qi Heng, one should begin with Taiyin. Treating according to what is not overcome (by the Five Elements) is considered adverse; if this occurs, death may follow. Treating in accordance with what is overcome is favorable; when this happens, recovery is possible. The Eight Winds and the Four Seasons' influences follow a cycle that ends and begins again; if this order is reversed even once, it cannot be counted or predicted. This concludes the essential discussion. 

16. 診要經終論 Essential Diagnosis and Meridian Exhaustion Theory

1
打開字典
	診要經終... :	
黃帝問曰：診要何如。歧伯對曰：正月二月，天氣始方，地氣始發，人氣在肝。三月四月，天氣正方，地氣定發，人氣在脾。五月六月，天氣盛，地氣高，人氣在頭。七月八月，陰氣始殺，人氣在肺。九月十月，陰氣始冰，地氣始閉，人氣在心。十一月十二月，冰復，地氣合，人氣在腎。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: What is essential for diagnosis? Qibo replied: In the first and second months, the celestial qi begins to expand, the terrestrial qi begins to emerge, and human qi resides in the liver. In the third and fourth months, the celestial qi is fully expanding, the terrestrial qi firmly emerges, and human qi resides in the spleen. In the fifth and sixth months, the celestial qi thrives, the terrestrial qi rises high, and human qi resides in the head. In the seventh and eighth months, yin qi begins to wane, and human qi resides in the lungs. In the ninth and tenth months, yin qi starts to freeze, the terrestrial qi begins to close, and human qi resides in the heart. In the eleventh and twelfth months, ice returns, the terrestrial qi unites, and human qi resides in the kidneys.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	診要經終... :	
故春刺散俞，及與分理，血出而止，甚者傳氣，閒者環也。夏刺絡俞，見血而止，盡氣閉環，痛病必下。秋刺皮膚，循理，上下同法，神變而止。冬刺俞竅於分理，甚者直下，閒者散下。春夏秋冬，各有所刺，法其所在。
		Therefore, in spring, acupuncture should be applied to dispersed 俞 points and along the interstices of the body's meridians; bleeding should cease once achieved. In severe cases, qi transmission is involved; in milder cases, it forms a circular pattern. In summer, acupuncture should target the collateral 俞 points, and bleeding should cease once blood is seen; this exhausts qi and closes the ring. Painful conditions will then descend. In autumn, acupuncture should penetrate the skin and follow the body's interstices; the method is the same for upper and lower parts of the body, ceasing only when a transformation in spirit occurs. In winter, acupuncture should be applied at 俞 points along the interstices; in severe cases, it proceeds directly downward, and in milder cases, disperses downward. Each season—spring, summer, autumn, and winter—has its own acupuncture method; the approach follows where qi resides.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	診要經終... :	
春刺夏分，脈亂氣微，入淫骨髓，病不能愈，令人不嗜食，又且少氣。春刺秋分，筋攣，逆氣環，為欬嗽，病不愈，令人時驚，又且哭。春刺冬分，邪氣著藏，令人脹，病不愈，又且欲言語。夏刺春分，病不愈，令人解墯。夏刺秋分，病不愈，令人心中欲無言，惕惕如人將補之。夏刺冬分，病不愈，令人少氣，時欲怒。秋刺春分，病不已，令人惕然欲有所為，起而忘之。秋刺夏分，病不已，令人益嗜臥，又且善㝱。秋刺冬分，病不已，令人洒洒時寒。冬刺春分，病不已，令人欲臥不能眠，眠而有見。冬刺夏分，病不愈，氣上，發為諸痺。冬刺秋分，病不已，令人善渴。
		If needling is performed in spring for summer conditions, the pulse becomes disordered and qi weakens; pathogenic influences infiltrate the bones and marrow, illness cannot be cured, and one loses appetite while also experiencing a deficiency of qi. If needling is performed in spring to address autumn conditions, the tendons become contracted and qi becomes reversed and circular, causing coughing; illness cannot be cured, one experiences sudden startlements, and also weeps. If needling is performed in spring targeting winter conditions, pathogenic qi lodges in the zang organs, causing distension; illness cannot be cured and one feels an urge to speak. If needling is performed in summer targeting spring conditions, illness will not be cured, and the person may experience weakness or collapse. If needling is performed in summer to address autumn conditions, illness will not be cured; it causes a desire for silence within the heart and an anxious feeling as if someone were about to punish one. If needling is performed in summer addressing winter conditions, illness will not be cured and the person experiences a deficiency of qi along with occasional anger. If needling is performed in autumn targeting spring conditions, illness does not cease; it causes a person to feel restless and as if they want to do something, yet upon arising, they forget what they intended. If needling is performed in autumn addressing summer conditions, illness does not subside; it causes a person to desire more sleep and also become prone to drowsiness. If needling is performed in autumn for winter conditions, the illness does not cease; it makes a person feel chilly at times with a shivering sensation. If needling is performed in winter targeting spring conditions, the illness does not subside; one feels drowsy yet cannot sleep, and when sleeping, experiences dreams or visions. 偻 If needling is performed in winter to address summer conditions, the illness will not be cured; qi rises upward and manifests as various types of bi syndrome (bi being a term for obstruction or stagnation causing pain and numbness). If needling is performed in winter addressing autumn conditions, the illness does not stop; it causes one to become prone to thirst.

4
打開字典
	診要經終... :	
凡刺胸腹者，必避五藏。中心者，環死；中脾者，五日死；中腎者，七日死；中肺者，五日死；中鬲者，皆為傷中，其病雖愈，不過一歲必死。刺避五藏者，知逆從也。所謂從者，鬲與脾腎之處，不知者反之。刺胸腹者，必以布憿著之，乃從單布上刺，刺之不愈，復刺。刺鍼必肅，刺腫搖鍼，經刺勿搖，此刺之道也。
		Whenever acupuncture is applied to the chest and abdomen, one must avoid the five zang organs. Piercing the heart center results in a ring of death; Piercing the spleen causes death within five days; Piercing the kidneys causes death within seven days; Piercing the lungs causes death within five days; Piercing the diaphragm results in internal injury. Although the illness may recover, one will inevitably die within a year. Knowing how to avoid piercing the five zang organs is understanding the principles of following and opposing qi flow. What is meant by "following" is that, in relation to the diaphragm and the locations of the spleen and kidneys, those who do not understand should reverse their approach. When needling the chest or abdomen, one must place a cloth pad over the area and insert the needle through a single layer of cloth. If the condition does not improve after needling, repeat the procedure. When inserting needles, one must be solemn; when needling a swelling, gently rock the needle. When needling meridians, do not rock the needle—this is the principle of acupuncture.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	診要經終... :	
帝曰：願聞十二經脈之終，奈何。歧伯曰：太陽之脈，其終也戴眼反折瘈瘲，其色白，絕汗乃出，出則死矣。少陽終者，耳聾百節皆縱，目𦊷絕系，絕系一日半死，其死也，色先青白，乃死矣。陽明終者，口目動作，善驚妄言，色黃，其上下經盛，不仁，則終矣。少陰終者，面黑齒長而垢，腹脹閉，上下不通而終矣。太陰終者，腹脹閉不得息，善噫善嘔，嘔則逆，逆則面赤，不逆則上下不通，不通則面黑皮毛焦而終矣。厥陰終者，中熱嗌乾，善溺心煩，甚則舌卷卵上縮而終矣。此十二經之所敗也。
		The Emperor said: I wish to hear about how the twelve regular meridians reach their end, what is it like? Qibo said: The Taiyang meridian, when reaching its end, causes the eyes to roll upward and limbs to twist with convulsions; the complexion turns white, profuse cold sweat emerges, and once this occurs, death follows. The Shaoyang meridian, when reaching its end: causes deafness and relaxation of all joints; the eyes roll upward with severed tendons. Once the tendons are severed, death occurs within one and a half days. When it happens, the complexion first turns blue-white before death ensues. The Yangming meridian, when reaching its end: causes movement of the mouth and eyes, frequent startlements, delirious speech; the complexion turns yellow. When both upper and lower meridians are full and sensation is lost, it signifies the end has come. The Shaoyin meridian, when reaching its final stage: causes a dark facial complexion, elongated and dirty teeth, abdominal distension with obstruction; the passage of both upper and lower functions ceases, marking the end. The Taiyin meridian, when reaching the end: causes abdominal distension and obstruction with inability to breathe properly; frequent belching and vomiting occur. Vomiting leads to reversal of qi flow, which results in a red face. If there is no such reversal, then both upper and lower passages become blocked. When this blockage occurs, the complexion turns black, skin and hair dry out, marking the end. The Jueyin meridian, when at its final stage: causes internal heat and a dry throat; frequent urination and mental restlessness occur. In severe cases, the tongue curls upward and the testicles retract toward the body, marking the end. These are the signs of failure in the twelve meridians. 

17. 脈要精微論 Essential Subtleties of Pulse Diagnosis Theory

1
打開字典
	脈要精微... :	
黃帝問曰：診法何如。歧伯對曰：診法常以平旦，陰氣未動，陽氣未散，飲食未進，經脈未盛，絡脈調勻，氣血未亂，故乃可診有過之脈。切脈動靜而視精明，察五色，觀五藏有餘不足，六府強弱，形之盛衰，以此參伍決死生之分。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: How is diagnosis conducted? Qibo replied: The method of diagnosis is usually conducted at dawn, when the yin qi has not yet stirred and the yang qi has not dispersed; before food or drink are consumed, when the meridians have not yet become active, the collateral vessels are in harmony, and the qi and blood have not yet become disordered. Therefore, it is then possible to diagnose pulses that indicate excess conditions. By palpating the pulses to discern their movement and stillness, observing the clarity of the eyes, examining the five colors, assessing whether the five zang organs are in excess or deficient, determining the strength or weakness of the six fu organs, and evaluating the body's vitality or decline, one can thereby cross-reference these signs to decide between life and death.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	脈要精微... :	
夫脈者，血之府也，長則氣治，短則氣病，數則煩心，大則病進，上盛則氣高，下盛則氣脹，代則氣衰，細則氣少，濇則心痛，渾渾革至如涌泉，病進而色弊，緜緜其去如弦絕，死。夫精明五色者，氣之華也。赤欲如白裹朱，不欲如赭；白欲如鵝羽，不欲如鹽；青欲如蒼璧之澤，不欲如藍；黃欲如羅裹雄黃，不欲如黃土；黑欲如重漆色，不欲如地蒼。五色精微象見矣，其壽不久也。夫精明者，所以視萬物，別白黑，審短長。以長為短，以白為黑，如是則精衰矣。
		The pulse is the residence of blood. When it is long, the qi is harmonious; when short, there is a disease in the qi. A rapid pulse causes mental restlessness; a large pulse indicates worsening illness. An upper excess suggests elevated qi; a lower excess implies distension. A substituting (intermittent) pulse signifies declining qi; a fine pulse means deficient qi. A 涩 (sè) pulse indicates chest pain. When the pulse suddenly becomes strong and surging like a gushing spring, it signals worsening illness and deteriorating complexion. If the pulse gradually fades away as if a string were broken, death will occur. The clarity of the eyes and the five colors are the brilliance of qi. A red color should resemble cinnabar wrapped in white, not like ochre; White should resemble goose feathers, not salt; Green should resemble the luster of a blue jade disk, not indigo; Yellow should resemble orpiment wrapped in gauze, not yellow earth; Black should resemble the deep lacquer color, not the gray of the ground. When the subtle manifestations of these five colors become apparent, their life will not be long. The clarity of the eyes is what allows one to observe all things, distinguish between white and black, and discern length and shortness. To perceive long as short, or white as black—when this occurs, the essence is declining.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	脈要精微... :	
藏者，中之守也，中盛藏滿，氣勝傷恐者，聲如從室中言，是中氣之濕也。言而微，終日乃復言者，此奪氣也。衣被不斂，言語善惡，不避親踈者，此神明之亂也。倉廩不藏者，是門戶不要也。水泉不止者，是膀胱不藏也。得守者生，失守者死。夫五藏者，身之強也，頭者精明之府，頭傾視深，精神將奪矣。背者胸中之府，背曲肩隨，府將壞矣。腰者腎之府，轉搖不能，腎將憊矣。膝者筋之府，屈伸不能，行則僂附，筋將憊矣。骨者髓之府，不能久立，行則振掉，骨將憊矣。得強則生，失強則死。
		The zang organs are the guardians of the center (the body's core). When the center is strong and the zang full, excessive qi may cause injury or fear. The voice sounds as if speaking from within a room—this indicates dampness in the central qi. When one speaks faintly and only after an entire day speaks again, this is indicative of loss of qi. When one does not cover up with clothing or bedding and speaks inappropriately, saying whatever comes to mind without distinguishing between close relatives and strangers, this indicates a disorder of the spirit and clarity. When granaries do not store properly, it is as if the gates and doors are unguarded. When water sources cannot be controlled, it indicates that the bladder does not retain properly. Those who maintain their defenses live; those who lose them die. The five zang organs are the source of bodily strength. The head is the residence of the clarity and brightness (of the spirit). When the head droops and one looks deeply, it indicates that spiritual energy is about to be lost. The back is the residence of the chest cavity. When the back curves and the shoulders droop, it indicates that this residence is about to collapse. The waist is the residence of the kidneys; when one cannot turn or move it freely, the kidneys are about to become exhausted. The knees are the residence of tendons; when one cannot bend or straighten them, and walking causes hunching over, it indicates that the tendons are about to become exhausted. The bones are the residence of marrow; when one cannot stand for long and walking causes trembling, it indicates that the bones are about to become exhausted. Those who retain their strength live; those who lose it die.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	脈要精微... :	
歧伯曰：反四時者，有餘為精，不足為消。應太過，不足為精；應不足，有餘為消。陰陽不相應，病名曰關格。帝曰：脈其四時動柰何，知病之所在柰何，知病之所變奈何，知病乍在內柰何，知病乍在外奈何，請問此五者，可得聞乎。歧伯曰：請言其與天運轉大也。萬物之外，六合之內，天地之變，陰陽之應，彼春之暖，為夏之暑，彼秋之忿，為冬之怒，四變之動，脈與之上下，以春應中規，夏應中矩，秋應中衡，冬應中權。是故冬至四十五日，陽氣微上，陰氣微下；夏至四十五日，陰氣微上，陽氣微下。陰陽有時，與脈為期，期而相失，知脈所分，分之有期，故知死時。微妙在脈，不可不察，察之有紀，從陰陽始，始之有經，從五行生，生之有度，四時為宜，補寫勿失，與天地如一，得一之情，以知死生。是故聲合五音，色合五行，脈合陰陽，是知陰盛則夢涉大水恐懼，陽盛則夢大火燔灼，陰陽俱盛則夢相殺毀傷；上盛則夢飛，下盛則夢墮；甚飽則夢予，甚飢則夢取；肝氣盛則夢怒，肺氣盛則夢哭；短蟲多則夢聚眾，長蟲多則夢相擊毀傷。是故持脈有道，虛靜為保。春日浮，如魚之遊在波；夏日在膚，泛泛乎萬物有餘；秋日下膚，蟄蟲將去；冬日在骨，蟄蟲周密，君子居室。故曰：知內者按而紀之，知外者終而始之。此六者，持脈之大法。
		Qibo said: Those whose conditions contradict the four seasons, if they have an excess, it is due to essence; if a deficiency, it indicates depletion. If there is excessive response, it does not indicate essence; If the response is insufficient, an excess indicates depletion. When yin and yang do not correspond with each other, the disease is called Guange (obstruction). The Yellow Emperor asked: How does the pulse change with the four seasons? How can one know where a disease is located? How can one discern how a disease changes? How can one recognize when a disease suddenly appears internally? And how can one determine when it suddenly manifests externally? I ask about these five matters—may I hear your explanation? Qibo said: Please allow me to explain how they correspond with the great movements of Heaven. All things outside, within the 六合 (six directions), undergo changes of Heaven and Earth, with yin and yang responding accordingly. The warmth of spring becomes the heat of summer; the resentment of autumn becomes the fury of winter. These four transformations affect movement, and the pulse rises and falls in accordance. In spring, it corresponds to the compass (circle); in summer, to the square (rectangle); in autumn, to the balance beam; and in winter, to the scale weight. Therefore, on the fifteenth day after the Winter Solstice (December 21), yang qi begins to rise slightly while yin qi descends slightly; on the fifteenth day after the Summer Solstice (June 21), yin qi begins to rise slightly and yang qi descends slightly. Yin and yang have their times, and the pulse corresponds accordingly. When they fail to meet as expected, one can discern where the pulse is divided. Since division occurs at a predictable time, therefore one can know when death will occur. The subtlety lies in the pulse; it cannot be neglected. When examining, one must follow a principle—beginning with yin and yang. The beginning follows a pattern derived from the Five Elements. Their generation has limits, appropriate to the four seasons. Do not miss the timing of supplementation or reduction. Aligning with Heaven and Earth as one, understanding this unity allows knowledge of life and death. Therefore, the voice corresponds to the Five Tones, color to the Five Elements, and pulse to yin and yang. Thus one knows that when yin is excessive, dreams involve crossing great waters with fear; when yang is excessive, dreams of a great fire burning fiercely; when both yin and yang are excessive, dreams involve mutual killing and destruction; when the upper part is excessive, one dreams of flying; when the lower part is excessive, one dreams of falling. When excessively full, one dreams of giving; when extremely hungry, one dreams of taking; when the liver qi is excessive, one dreams of anger; when the lung qi is excessive, one dreams of weeping; an abundance of small worms causes dreams of gatherings and crowds, while an excess of long worms causes dreams of mutual combat and injury. Therefore, there is a method to holding the pulse; emptiness and stillness are essential safeguards. In spring, the pulse floats lightly, like fish swimming in waves; in summer it resides on the skin surface, appearing abundant and expansive as if all things are in excess; in autumn it sinks beneath the skin, like hibernating insects preparing to depart; in winter it resides in the bones, as hibernating insects remain hidden and secure, just as a gentleman stays within his home. Therefore it is said: Those who understand the interior examine and record accordingly; those who understand the exterior begin with the end in mind. These six principles are the fundamental methods of pulse diagnosis.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	脈要精微... :	
心脈搏堅而長，當病舌卷不能言；其耎而散者，當消環自已。肺脈搏堅而長，當病唾血；其耎而散者，當病灌汗，至今不復散發也。肝脈搏堅而長，色不青，當病墜若搏，因血在脅下，令人喘逆；其耎而散色澤者，當病溢飲，溢飲者渴暴多飲，而易入肌皮腸胃之外也。胃脈搏堅而長，其色赤，當病折髀；其耎而散者，當病食痺。脾脈搏堅而長，其色黃，當病少氣；其耎而散色不澤者，當病足䯒腫，若水狀也。腎脈搏堅而長，其色黃而赤者，當病折腰；其耎而散者，當病少血，至今不復也。帝曰：診得心脈而急，此為何病，病形何如。歧伯曰：病名心疝，少腹當有形也。帝曰：何以言之。歧伯曰：心為牡藏，小腸為之使，故曰少腹當有形也。帝曰：診得胃脈，病形何如。歧伯曰：胃脈實則脹，虛則泄。
		If the heart pulse is forceful and long, it indicates illness in which the tongue curls up and speech becomes impossible; if the pulse is soft and diffused, the condition will naturally resolve on its own. If the lung pulse is forceful and long, one should expect illness involving blood in the sputum; if it is soft and diffused instead, the condition will involve profuse sweating that persists without resolution. If the liver pulse is forceful and long but the complexion does not appear green, it indicates a condition of falling injury or trauma, with blood accumulating beneath the ribs, causing shortness of breath and upward rushing qi; if it is soft, diffused, yet the complexion appears lustrous, it indicates excessive fluid accumulation. In such cases of "yi yin" (overflowing drink), one experiences sudden intense thirst and drinks a lot, but the fluids easily spread to the skin, muscles, and outside the intestines and stomach. If the stomach pulse is forceful and long with a red complexion, it indicates an injury to the hip joint; if it is soft and diffused, it indicates food-induced bi syndrome (a type of obstruction or blockage). If the spleen pulse is forceful and long and the complexion appears yellow, it indicates a deficiency in qi; if it is soft and diffused with a dull complexion, the condition will manifest as swelling of the feet resembling edema. If the kidney pulse is forceful and long while the complexion appears yellow with a reddish tinge, it indicates an injury to or fracture of the waist; if it is soft and diffused in nature, it indicates blood deficiency that will not recover. The Yellow Emperor asked: If the heart pulse is diagnosed as taut (tight), what disease does this indicate, and how will its symptoms manifest? Qibo said: The condition is called xin shan (heart hernia), and there should be a palpable mass in the lower abdomen. The Yellow Emperor asked: On what basis do you say this? Qibo said: The heart is the male zang organ, and the small intestine serves as its messenger; therefore, it is said that there should be a palpable form in the lower abdomen. The Yellow Emperor asked: If one diagnoses the stomach pulse, what symptoms will appear? Qibo said: When the stomach pulse is full, distension occurs; when it is deficient, diarrhea results.

	脈要精微... :	
帝曰：病成而變何謂。歧伯曰：風成為寒熱，癉成為消中，厥成為巔疾，久風為飱泄，脈風成為癘，病之變化，不可勝數。帝曰：諸癰腫筋攣骨痛，此皆安生。歧伯曰：此寒氣之腫，八風之變也。帝曰：治之奈何。歧伯曰：此四時之病，以其勝治之，愈也。帝曰：有故病五藏發動，因傷脈色，各何以知其久暴至之病乎。歧伯曰：悉乎哉問也。徵其脈小色不奪者，新病也；徵其脈不奪其色奪者，此久病也；徵其脈與五色俱奪者，此久病也；徵其脈與五色俱不奪者，新病也。肝與腎脈並至，其色蒼赤，當病毀傷，不見血，已見血，濕若中水也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: What does "disease formation and transformation" mean? Qibo said: Wind transforming gives rise to cold and heat; dampness transforming leads to xiao zhong (a condition of internal consumption); reversal (厥) transforming causes dian disease (head-related illness); prolonged wind results in loose stools after meals. Wind affecting the pulse leads to leprosy. The transformations of diseases are too numerous to count. The Yellow Emperor asked: All kinds of abscesses, swellings, muscle contractions, and bone pain—how do these conditions arise? Qibo said: These are swellings caused by cold qi and transformations due to the Eight Winds. The Yellow Emperor asked: How should these conditions be treated? Qibo said: These are diseases of the four seasons; they can be cured by treating them according to their opposing elements. The Yellow Emperor asked: When old illnesses of the five zang organs flare up, causing injury to pulse and complexion, how can one distinguish between chronic or sudden-onset diseases? Qibo said: What a thorough question this is. If the pulse is small but the complexion remains unchanged, it indicates a new illness; if the pulse shows no change yet the complexion has altered, this indicates a long-standing illness; if both the pulse and the five complexions are diminished, it is also an indication of chronic disease; if neither the pulse nor the five complexions show signs of depletion, then it indicates a recent illness. When the pulses of the liver and kidneys arrive together, with a complexion appearing blue-red, it indicates injury or damage. Even if no blood is visible at first, eventually blood will appear, as though soaked in water.

	脈要精微... :	
尺內兩傍，則季脅也，尺外以候腎，尺裏以候腹中。附上左，外以候肝，內以候鬲；右，外以候胃，內以候脾。上附上右，外以候肺，內以候胸中；左，外以候心，內以候膻中。前以候前，後以候後。上竟上者，胸喉中事也；下竟下者，少腹腰股膝脛足中事也。
		The two sides within the chi (cubit) region correspond to the hypochondriac regions; the outer part of the chi is used to diagnose the kidneys, while the inner part corresponds to conditions in the abdomen. Above the chi on the left side, the exterior portion is used to examine the liver, and the interior portion examines the diaphragm; on the right side, the exterior part is for examining the stomach, while the interior part corresponds to the spleen. Above the upper portion on the right, the exterior is used to examine the lungs, and the interior examines the chest cavity; on the left side, the exterior corresponds to the heart, while the interior corresponds to the shanzhong (region of the pericardium). The front part is used to examine conditions in the anterior body; the back part examines those in the posterior. The uppermost portion corresponds to the chest and throat region. The lowermost part corresponds to conditions of the lower abdomen, waist, thighs, knees, shins, and feet.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	脈要精微... :	
麤大者，陰不足陽有餘，為熱中也。來疾去徐，上實下虛，為厥巔疾；來徐去疾，上虛下實，為惡風也。故中惡風者，陽氣受也。有脈俱沈細數者，少陰厥也；沈細數散者，寒熱也；浮而散者為眴仆。諸浮不躁者皆在陽，則為熱，其有躁者在手。諸細而沈者皆在陰，則為骨痛；其有靜者在足。數動一代者，病在陽之脈也，洩及便膿血。諸過者，切之，濇者陽氣有餘也，滑者陰氣有餘也。陽氣有餘，為身熱无汗，陰氣有餘，為多汗身寒，陰陽有餘，則无汗而寒。推而外之，內而不外，有心腹積也。推而內之，外而不內，身有熱也。推而上之，上而不下，腰足清也。推而下之，下而不上，頭項痛也。按之至骨，脈氣少者，腰脊痛而身有痺也。
		A coarse and large pulse indicates yin deficiency with excessive yang; this is a condition of internal heat. When the pulse arrives quickly but departs slowly, it suggests excess above and deficiency below—this indicates reversal (厥) disease or head-related illness; when the pulse comes slowly but departs quickly, indicating deficiency above and excess below—it is a sign of sensitivity to wind. Therefore, those who suffer from aversion to wind have an affliction in their yang qi. When the pulse is deep, fine, and rapid, it indicates reversal (厥) of Shaoyin; a deep, fine, rapid, and diffused pulse indicates cold-heat syndrome; A floating and diffused pulse signifies dizziness or fainting. All floating pulses that are not rapid indicate conditions in the yang aspect, which belong to heat; those with a rapid quality occur in the hands. All fine and deep pulses reside within yin, indicating bone pain; those with a calm pulse are found in the feet. A rapid, forceful pulse followed by an intermittent beat indicates disease in yang meridians; it is associated with diarrhea and discharge of pus or blood. All excessive conditions, when palpated: if the pulse feels 涩 (sè), it indicates excess yang qi; if 滑 (huá), it signifies excess yin qi. Excess yang qi causes body heat with no perspiration; excess yin qi results in excessive sweating and coldness. When both yin and yang are in excess, there will be no sweat but a sensation of cold. If the pulse is pushed outward but remains inward without going out, it indicates accumulation in the heart and abdomen. Pushing the pulse inward while it remains on the exterior without entering signifies fever in the body. Pushing upward and finding that the pulse moves upward but not downward indicates coldness in the waist and feet. Pushing downward and finding that it goes down without rising signifies pain in the head and neck. Pressing until reaching the bone, if the pulse qi is weak, indicates lower back and spine pain with bodily numbness. 

18. 平人氣象論 On the Vital Signs of a Healthy Person

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	平人氣象... :	
黃帝問曰：平人何如。歧伯對曰：人一呼脈再動，一吸脈亦再動，呼吸定息脈五動，閏以太息，命曰平人。平人者，不病也。常以不病調病人，醫不病，故為病人平息以調之為法。人一呼脈一動，一吸脈一動，曰少氣。人一呼脈三動，一吸脈三動而躁，尺熱曰病溫，尺不熱脈滑曰病風，脈濇曰痺。人一呼脈四動以上曰死，脈絕不至曰死，乍踈乍數曰死。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: What is a healthy person like? Qibo replied: When a person exhales once, the pulse moves twice; when he inhales once, the pulse also moves twice. After one complete breath cycle of inhalation and exhalation, the pulse moves five times. If there is an additional movement with a deep sigh, it is called a healthy person. A healthy person is one who does not have illness. One should always use the condition of a healthy person to regulate that of an ill patient. A physician, being free from illness himself, uses the normal breathing and rest patterns of a healthy individual as a standard to adjust and treat the patient accordingly. When a person exhales once and the pulse moves once, and inhales once and the pulse also moves once, it is called "lack of vital energy" (shao qi). When a person exhales once and his pulse moves three times, and inhales once with the pulse also moving three times in an agitated manner, if the cun (inch) portion of the wrist is warm, it indicates a febrile disease. If the cun is not warm but the pulse is slippery, it indicates wind illness; if the pulse is rough or 涩 (se), it indicates bi syndrome (bi). When a person exhales once and their pulse moves four times or more, it indicates death. If the pulse is completely absent and does not arrive at all, it also indicates death. A pulse that alternates between irregularly slow (zha shu) and fast (zha shu), fluctuating unpredictably, also indicates death.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	平人氣象... :	
平人之常氣稟於胃，胃者，平人之常氣也，人无胃氣曰逆，逆者死。春胃微弦曰平，弦多胃少曰肝病，但弦无胃曰死，胃而有毛曰秋病，毛甚曰今病。藏真散於肝，肝藏勱膜之氣也。夏胃微鉤曰平，鉤多胃少曰心病，但鉤无胃曰死，胃而有石曰冬病，石甚曰今病。藏真通於心，心藏血脈之氣也。長夏胃微耎弱曰平，弱多胃少曰脾病，但代无胃曰死，耎弱有石曰冬病，弱甚曰今病。藏真濡於脾，脾藏肌肉之氣也。秋胃微毛曰平，毛多胃少曰肺病，但毛无胃曰死，毛而有弦曰春病，弦甚曰今病。藏真高於肺，以行榮衛陰陽也。冬胃微石曰平，石多胃少曰腎病，但石无胃曰死，石而有鉤曰夏病，鉤甚曰今病。藏真下於腎，腎藏骨髓之氣也。胃之大絡，名曰虛里，貫鬲絡肺，出於左乳下，其動應衣，脈宗氣也。盛喘數絕者，則病在中；結而橫，有積矣；絕不至曰死。乳之下其動應衣，宗氣泄也。
		The normal vital energy of a healthy person is derived from the stomach. The stomach is the source of this normal vital energy in a healthy individual. When a person lacks stomach qi, it is called "reversal" (ni), and reversal indicates death. In spring, a slightly wiry pulse with the quality of stomach qi is considered normal. If the wiry aspect predominates over the stomach qi, it indicates liver disease; if there is only a wiry pulse without any sign of stomach qi, it signifies death. When the stomach pulse has a slight hair-like (máo) quality, it suggests an illness that will occur in autumn; if this hair-like quality is pronounced, it means the illness is occurring now. The true essence stored in the liver disperses throughout the body; the liver stores the qi of the peritoneum (liver stores the qi that supports the membranes). In summer, a slightly hooked pulse with the quality of stomach qi indicates normalcy. If the hooked aspect predominates over stomach qi, it suggests heart disease; if there is merely a hooked pulse without any sign of stomach energy, it signifies death. A stomach pulse combined with a stone-like (shí) quality indicates an illness that will manifest in winter; if this stone-like quality is pronounced, the illness is occurring now. The true essence communicates through the heart; the heart stores the qi of blood and vessels. In the long summer, a slightly soft and weak pulse with the quality of stomach energy is considered normal. When weakness predominates over stomach energy, it indicates spleen disease; when there is only an intermittent (dài) pulse without any sign of stomach Qi, it signifies death. If a soft and weak pulse has a stone-like component, it suggests illness in winter; if the weakness is pronounced, then the illness occurs now. The true essence is moistened by the spleen; the spleen stores the qi of muscles. In autumn, a slightly hairy (máo) pulse with the quality of stomach Qi is considered normal. If hairiness predominates over stomach Qi, it indicates lung disease; if there is only the hairy aspect without any sign of stomach Qi at all, it signifies death. A hairy pulse combined with wiry (xián) characteristics suggests an illness that will appear in spring; if the wiry quality is pronounced, then the illness manifests now. The true essence resides above in the lungs to regulate the functions of nutritive and defensive qi as well as yin and yang. In winter, a slightly stone-like (shí) pulse with the quality of Stomach Qi is considered normal. When the stone-like aspect predominates over Stomach Qi, it indicates kidney disease; when there is merely a stone-like pulse without any sign of Stomach Qi at all, it signifies death.A stone-like pulse combined with a hooked (gōu) characteristic suggests an illness that will manifest in summer; if the hooked quality is pronounced, then the disease occurs now. The true essence descends to the kidneys; the kidneys store the qi of bone marrow. The major collateral of the stomach is called Xuli, which passes through the diaphragm and connects to the lungs. It emerges below the left nipple; its movement can be felt against clothing, representing the pulse of zong qi (primordial energy). If the breathing is forceful and rapid with intermittent stops, then the disease lies in the middle jiao. If the pulse is obstructed and feels transversely bound, there must be an accumulation (of pathogenic factors). When the pulse ceases entirely and does not arrive at all, death occurs. The movement below the nipple that can be felt against clothing indicates an overflow of zong qi.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	平人氣象... :	
欲知寸口太過與不及，寸口之脈中手短者，曰頭痛。寸口脈中手長者，曰足脛痛。寸口脈中手促上擊者，曰肩背痛。寸口脈沈而堅者，曰病在中。寸口脈浮而盛者，曰病在外。寸口脈沈而弱，曰寒熱及疝瘕少腹痛。寸口脈沈而橫，曰脇下有積，腹中有橫積痛。寸口脈沈而喘，曰寒熱。脈盛滑堅者，曰病在外。脈小實而堅者，病在內。脈小弱以濇，謂之久病。脈滑浮而疾者，謂之新病。脈急者，曰疝瘕少腹痛。脈滑曰風。脈濇曰痺。緩而滑曰熱中。盛而緊曰脹。
		To determine whether the cun Kou pulse is excessive or insufficient, if the pulse at the cun kou is short and weak in the hand, it indicates headache. If the cunkou pulse feels long when palpated with the hand, it signifies pain in the lower legs and shins. When the Cun Kou pulse is felt to be rapid and striking upward in the hand, it indicates shoulder and back pain. If the cunkou pulse is deep and firm, the disease lies in the middle (jiao). If the Cun Kou pulse is floating and strong, it indicates that the illness is on the exterior. When the cunkou pulse is sinking and weak, it indicates cold-heat syndromes as well as hernia, abdominal masses, or pain in the lower abdomen. When the cunkou pulse is sunken and transversely bound, it indicates an accumulation beneath the flanks and a horizontally positioned mass in the abdomen causing pain. If the Cun Kou pulse feels deep and is accompanied by shortness of breath, it indicates cold-heat syndrome. A strong, slippery, and firm pulse indicates a disease on the exterior. If the pulse is small but solid and firm, it indicates an internal illness. A small, weak, and rough (se) pulse is indicative of a long-standing chronic illness. A slippery, floating, and rapid pulse indicates an acute or recent illness. A taut (jí) pulse indicates hernia, abdominal mass, or pain in the lower abdomen. A slippery pulse indicates wind-related illness. A rough (se) pulse indicates bi syndrome. If the pulse is slow and slippery, it indicates internal heat. A strong and tense (jin) pulse indicates distension or bloating.

4
打開字典
	平人氣象... :	
脈從陰陽，病易已；脈逆陰陽，病難已。脈得四時之順，曰病无他；脈反四時及不閒藏，曰難已。
		When the pulse follows the balance of yin and yang, the illness is easily resolved; When the pulse contradicts the harmony of yin and yang, it indicates a difficult-to-treat illness. If the pulse conforms to the normal seasonal patterns, it means there is no serious disease; When the pulse contradicts the four seasons or does not harmonize with the zang organs, it indicates a difficult illness to resolve.

5
打開字典
	平人氣象... :	
臂多青脈，曰脫血。尺脈緩濇，謂之解㑊，安臥脈盛，謂之脫血。尺濇脈滑，謂之多汗。尺寒脈細，謂之後泄。脈尺麤常熱者，謂之熱中。
		If there are many blue veins on the arm, it is indicative of blood loss (tuo xue). A slow and rough pulse at the chi position indicates fatigue and weakness. If a person lies down peacefully but has an excessively strong pulse, it is indicative of blood depletion (tuo xue). A rough (se) chi pulse combined with slipperiness indicates excessive sweating. If the chi pulse is cold and fine, it indicates post-defecation diarrhea or loose stools after meals. A coarse and persistently hot chi pulse indicates internal heat (re zhong).

6
打開字典
	平人氣象... :	
肝見庚辛死，心見壬癸死，脾見甲乙死，肺見丙丁死，腎見戊己死，是謂真藏見皆死。
		When the liver is affected by Geng or Xin (metal elements), death occurs; when the heart is affected by Ren or Gui (water elements), death occurs; when the spleen is affected by Jia or Yi (wood elements), death occurs; when the lungs are affected by Bing or Ding (fire elements), death occurs; and when the kidneys are affected by Wu or Ji (earth elements), death occurs. This is called "the true zang organs appearing," which indicates death.

7
打開字典
	平人氣象... :	
頸脈動喘疾欬，曰水。目裏微腫如臥蠶起之狀，曰水。溺黃赤安臥者，黃疸。已食如飢者，胃疸。面腫曰風。足脛腫曰水。目黃者曰黃疸。
		If the neck vessels pulsate with rapid breathing and coughing, it indicates fluid retention or edema (shui zheng). When there is slight swelling in the eyelids resembling the appearance of a resting silkworm, it indicates fluid retention or water disease (shui zheng). If the urine is yellowish-red and the person lies down calmly, it indicates jaundice. If one feels hungry immediately after eating, it indicates stomach jaundice (wei dan). Facial swelling is indicative of wind illness. Swelling in the feet and shins indicates fluid retention or water condition (shui zheng). Yellow eyes indicate jaundice.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	平人氣象... :	
婦人手少陰脈動甚者，妊子也。
		If the Shaoyin pulse in a woman's hand is markedly active, it indicates pregnancy.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	平人氣象... :	
脈有逆從四時，未有藏形，春夏而脈瘦，秋冬而脈浮大，命曰逆四時也。風熱而脈靜，泄而脫血脈實，病在中，脈虛，病在外，脈濇堅者，皆難治，命曰反四時也。
		There are pulses that contradict the four seasons; even without visible signs in the zang organs, if during spring and summer the pulse is thin or weak, and during autumn and winter it is floating and large, this is called "contradicting the four seasons," indicating an abnormal condition. When there is wind-heat but the pulse remains calm, or when there is diarrhea and blood loss yet the pulse feels full, it indicates an interior disease. When the condition lies on the exterior but the pulse appears weak, or when the pulse is rough and firm despite the illness, all these are difficult to treat; this is called "contradicting the four seasonal patterns."

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	平人氣象... :	
人以水穀為本，故人絕水穀則死，脈無胃氣亦死。所謂無胃氣者，但得真藏脈不得胃氣也。所謂脈不得胃氣者，肝不弦腎不石也。太陽脈至，洪大以長；少陽脈至，乍數乍踈，乍短乍長；陽明脈至，浮大而短。夫平心脈來，累累如連珠，如循琅玕，曰心平，夏以胃氣為本。病心脈來，喘喘連屬，其中微曲，曰心病。死心脈來，前曲後居，如操帶鉤，曰心死。平肺脈來，厭厭聶聶，如落榆莢，曰肺平，秋以胃氣為本。病肺脈來，不上不下，如循雞羽，曰肺病。死肺脈來，如物之浮，如風吹毛，曰肺死。平肝脈來，耎弱招招，如揭長竿末梢，曰肝平，春以胃氣為本。病肝脈來，盈實而滑，如循長竿，曰肝病。死肝脈來，急益勁，如新張弓弦，曰肝死。平脾脈來，和柔相離，如雞踐地，曰脾平，長夏以胃氣為本。病脾脈來，實而盈數，如雞舉足，曰脾病。死脾脈來，銳堅如烏之喙，如鳥之距，如屋之漏，如水之流，曰脾死。平腎脈來，喘喘累累如鉤，按之而堅，曰腎平，冬以胃氣為本。病腎脈來，如引葛，按之益堅，曰腎病。死腎脈來，發如奪索，辟辟如彈石，曰腎死。
		Human beings rely on water and grains as their foundation; therefore, when a person is deprived of water and food, they will die. Likewise, if the pulse lacks stomach (wei) qi, death also occurs. What is meant by "lack of Stomach Qi" is when only the true zang pulse is present without any indication of stomach qi. What is meant by a "pulse that lacks Stomach Qi" is when the liver does not exhibit a wiry (xian) quality and the kidneys do not show a stone-like (shi) pulse. When the Taiyang pulse arrives, it is large, full, and long; When the Shaoyang pulse arrives, it alternates between rapid (shu) and slow (chu), short and long. When the Yangming pulse arrives, it is floating, large, and short. The normal heart pulse comes in a continuous sequence like linked pearls or as if tracing the surface of langgan (a precious stone), and is called "heart harmony." In summer, it takes stomach qi as its foundation. When the heart pulse in a diseased condition arrives with rapid and continuous beats that are slightly curved or irregular, it indicates heart disease. When the heart pulse of a dying person comes forward with a curved shape and then remains stagnant, resembling the grip of a belt hook (cao dai gou), it is called "heart death." The normal lung pulse arrives gently and softly, like the falling of elm pods (yu jie), and is called "lung harmony." In autumn, it takes stomach Qi as its foundation. When the lung pulse in a diseased state neither rises nor falls, and feels like tracing chicken feathers, it indicates lung disease. The dying lung pulse comes floating as if something is drifting on water, or like wind blowing over fine hair; this is called "lung death." The normal liver pulse arrives soft and weak, gently waving like the tip of a long pole being lifted; this is called "liver harmony." In spring, it takes stomach Qi as its basis. When the liver pulse in a diseased state arrives full and slippery, like tracing along a long pole, it indicates liver disease. The dying liver pulse comes with increasing tension and rigidity, resembling the taut string of a newly drawn bow; this is called "liver death." The normal spleen pulse arrives in a harmonious and gentle manner, with intervals between beats like the steps of a chicken treading on the ground; this is called "spleen harmony." In the long summer season, it takes stomach Qi as its base. When the spleen pulse in a diseased state is full and rapid, like a chicken lifting its foot, it indicates spleen illness. The dying spleen pulse is sharp and firm, resembling the beak of a crow, the spur of a bird, or water dripping from a leaking roof—like flowing water—it is called "spleen death." The normal kidney pulse arrives with a steady, continuous flow like hooks, and feels firm when pressed; this is called "kidney harmony." In winter, it takes stomach Qi as its root. When the kidney pulse in a diseased state comes like pulling on kudzu (ge), and feels increasingly firm upon pressing, it indicates kidney illness. The dying kidney pulse comes suddenly like a rope being violently pulled, and sounds like striking stone with a sharp "popping" sound; this is called "kidney death." 

19. 玉機真藏論 Yuji Zhen Zang Lun

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	玉機真藏... :	
黃帝問曰：春脈如弦，何如而弦。歧伯對曰：春脈者肝也，東方木也，萬物之所以始生也，故其氣來，耎弱輕虛而滑，端直以長，故曰弦，反此者病。帝曰：何如而反。歧伯曰：其氣來實而強，此謂太過，病在外；其氣來不實而微，此謂不及，病在中。帝曰：春脈太過與不及，其病皆何如。歧伯曰：太過則令人善忘，忽忽眩冒而巔疾；其不及，則令人胸痛引背，下則兩脇胠滿。帝曰：善。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "Spring pulses resemble a bowstring; what is it like to be string-like?" Qibo replied: "The spring pulse corresponds to the liver, which belongs to wood in the east. It is the source of all things beginning to grow; therefore, its qi comes soft, weak, light, hollow and smooth, upright and long, hence it is called 'bowstring.' If it is contrary to this, there is disease." The Emperor asked: "What does it mean when it is the opposite?" Qibo said: "When its qi comes full and strong, this is called excessive, indicating disease on the exterior; " when its qi does not come full but is weak, this is termed insufficient, indicating disease within." The Emperor asked: "When the spring pulse is excessive or insufficient, what illnesses do they cause?" Qibo said: "If it is excessive, it causes people to forget easily, feel dazed and disoriented, and suffer from vertigo and diseases of the head; " if it is insufficient, it causes chest pain radiating to the back, and downward to fullness in both hypochondriac regions." The Emperor said: "Good."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	玉機真藏... :	
夏脈如鉤，何如而鉤。歧伯曰：夏脈者心也，南方火也，萬物之所以盛長也，故其氣來盛去衰，故曰鉤，反此者病。帝曰：何如而反。歧伯曰：其氣來盛去亦盛，此謂太過，病在外；其氣來不盛去反盛，此謂不及，病在中。帝曰：夏脈太過與不及，其病皆何如。歧伯曰：太過則令人身熱而膚痛，為浸淫；其不及，則令人煩心，上見欬唾，下為氣泄。帝曰：善。
		"The summer pulse resembles a hook; what is it like to resemble a hook?" Qibo said: "The summer pulse corresponds to the heart, which belongs to fire in the south. It is when all things flourish and grow; therefore, its arrival is strong while its departure is weak, hence it is called a 'hook.' If this is contrary, there will be disease." The Emperor asked: "What does it mean to be the opposite?" Qibo said: "When its arrival is strong and its departure is also strong, this is termed excessive, indicating disease on the surface; " when its arrival is not strong but its departure is instead strong, this is called insufficient, indicating illness within." The Emperor asked: "When the summer pulse is excessive or insufficient, respectively, what illnesses do they produce?" Qibo said: "If it is excessive, people will suffer from body heat and skin pain, resulting in spreading sores; " if it is insufficient, people will feel mentally distressed, with coughing and phlegm above, and qi deficiency diarrhea below." The Emperor said: "Good."

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	玉機真藏... :	
秋脈如浮，何如而浮。歧伯曰：秋脈者肺也，西方金也，萬物之所以收成也，故其氣來，輕虛以浮，來急去散，故曰浮，反此者病。帝曰：何如而反。歧伯曰：其氣來，毛而中央堅，兩傍虛，此謂太過，病在外；其氣來，毛而微，此謂不及，病在中。帝曰：秋脈太過與不及，其病皆何如。歧伯曰：太過則令人逆氣而背痛，慍慍然；其不及，則令人喘，呼吸少氣而欬，上氣見血，下聞病音。帝曰：善。
		"The autumn pulse is like floating; what does it mean to float?" Qibo said: "The autumn pulse corresponds to the lungs, which belong to metal in the west. It is when all things are harvested; therefore, its qi arrives light and hollow with a floating quality, coming quickly but dispersing upon departure, hence it is called floating. If this is contrary, disease will occur." The Emperor asked: "What does the opposite mean?" Qibo said: "When its qi arrives with a feathery texture and is firm in the center while hollow on both sides, this is termed excessive, signifying disease on the exterior; " when its qi arrives feathery yet faint, this is called insufficient, signifying illness within." The Emperor asked: "When the autumn pulse is excessive or insufficient respectively, what illnesses do they each cause?" Qibo said: "If it is excessive, one will suffer from counterflow of qi and back pain, feeling irritable; " if it is insufficient, one will experience shortness of breath, shallow breathing with coughing, blood in the sputum above, and audible signs of illness below." The Emperor said: "Good."

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	玉機真藏... :	
冬脈如營，何如而營。歧伯曰：各脈者腎也，北方水也，萬物之所以合藏也，故其氣來，沈以搏，故曰營，反此者病。帝曰：何如而反。歧伯曰：其氣來如彈石者，此謂太過，病在外；其去如數者，此謂不及，病在中。帝曰：冬脈太過與不及，其病皆何如。歧伯曰：太過，則令人解㑊，脊脈痛而少氣不欲言；其不及，則令人心懸如病飢，䏚中清，脊中痛，少腹滿，小便變。帝曰：善。
		"The winter pulse is like a tent; what does it mean for the pulse to resemble a tent?" Qibo said: "The winter pulse corresponds to the kidneys, which belong to water in the north. It is when all things converge and are stored; therefore, its qi arrives deep and forceful, hence it is called like a tent. If this is contrary, illness will occur." The Emperor asked: "What does the opposite mean?" Qibo said: "When its qi comes like a stone being struck, this is termed excessive and indicates disease on the exterior; " when it departs rapidly, this is called insufficient and indicates illness within." The Emperor asked: "When the winter pulse is excessive or insufficient respectively, which illnesses do they cause?" Qibo said: "If it is excessive, a person will feel weak and exhausted, suffer from back pain along the spine, have shortness of breath and be reluctant to speak; " if it is insufficient, a person will feel as if the heart is suspended and suffer from hunger-like discomfort, have coldness in the chest, pain along the spine, fullness in the lower abdomen, and changes in urination." The Emperor said: "Good."

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	玉機真藏... :	
帝曰：四時之序，逆從之變異也，然脾脈獨何主。歧伯曰：脾脈者土也，孤藏以灌四傍者也。帝曰：然則脾善惡，可得見之乎。歧伯曰：善者不可得見，惡者可見。帝曰：惡者何如可見。歧伯曰：其來如水之流者，此謂太過，病在外；如鳥之喙者，此謂不及，病在中。帝曰：夫子言脾為孤藏，中央土以灌四傍，其太過與不及，其病皆何如。歧伯曰：太過，則令人四支不舉；其不及，則令人九竅不通，名曰重強。帝瞿然而起，再拜而稽首曰：善。吾得脈之大要，天下至數，五色脈變，揆度奇恒，道在於一，神轉不迴，迴則不轉，及失其機，至數之要，迫近以微，著之玉版，藏之藏府，每旦讀之，名曰玉機。
		The Emperor asked: "The sequence of the four seasons and their variations in harmony or disharmony have been discussed. However, what does the spleen pulse specifically govern?" Qibo said: "The spleen pulse corresponds to earth; it is an isolated zang organ that nourishes the four sides." The Emperor asked: "Then, can one observe whether the spleen is healthy or unhealthy?" Qibo said: "When it is healthy, its condition cannot be directly observed; when it is diseased, one can see the signs." The Emperor asked: "What does a diseased spleen look like?" Qibo said: "When its pulse comes like flowing water, this is termed excessive and signifies disease on the exterior; " when it resembles a bird's beak, this is called insufficient and signifies illness within." The Emperor asked: "You said the spleen is an isolated zang, corresponding to central earth that nourishes all four sides. What illnesses arise when its condition is excessive or insufficient?" Qibo said: "If it is excessive, the person will be unable to move their limbs; " if it is insufficient, the person will suffer from blockage in all nine orifices, a condition known as 'zhong qiang'." The Emperor started up with alarm, bowed twice and kowtowed, saying: "Good." "I have now grasped the essential principles of pulse diagnosis, which are among the most profound teachings in the world. The changes in five colors and pulses, as well as the measurement and assessment of normal and abnormal conditions, all derive from a single principle. The divine mechanism turns without reversal; if it reverses, then it cannot turn again, thus missing its opportunity. This essential teaching is subtle yet imminent. I will inscribe it on jade tablets, store them in the imperial archives, read them every morning, and call this collection 'Yu Ji'."

6
打開字典
	玉機真藏... :	
藏受氣於其所生，傳之於其所勝，氣舍於其所生，死於其所不勝。病之且死，必先傳行至其所不勝，病乃死。此言氣之逆行也，故死。肝受氣於心，傳之於脾，氣舍於腎，至肺而死。心受氣於脾，傳之於肺，氣舍於肝，至腎而死。脾受氣於肺，傳之於腎，氣舍於心，至肝而死。肺受氣於腎，傳之於肝，氣舍於脾，至心而死。腎受氣於肝，傳之於心，氣舍於肺，至脾而死。此皆逆死也。一日一夜五分之，此所以占死生之早暮也。
		The zang organs receive qi from their generating elements, transmit it to the elements they overcome. Qi resides in what generates them and perishes in what overcomes them. When a disease is about to result in death, it will first spread to the element that overcomes it; then the person dies. This describes the reversal of qi movement, hence leading to death. The liver receives qi from the heart and transmits it to the spleen; its qi resides in the kidneys, but when it reaches the lungs, death occurs. The heart receives qi from the spleen and transmits it to the lungs; its qi resides in the liver, but when it reaches the kidneys, death occurs. The spleen receives qi from the lungs and transmits it to the kidneys; its qi resides in the heart, but when it reaches the liver, death occurs. The lungs receive qi from the kidneys and transmit it to the liver; its qi resides in the spleen, but when it reaches the heart, death occurs. The kidneys receive qi from the liver and transmit it to the heart; its qi resides in the lungs, but when it reaches the spleen, death occurs. These are all cases of death due to reversal. Dividing one day and night into five parts allows the determination of whether death or recovery will occur early or late.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	玉機真藏... :	
黃帝曰：五藏相通，移皆有次，五藏有病，則各傳其所勝。不治，法三月若六月，若三日若六日，傳五藏而當死，是順傳所勝之次。故曰：別於陽者，知病從來；別於陰者，知死生之期。言知至其所困而死。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "The five zang organs are interconnected, and their transmission follows a sequence. When one of the five zang organs is diseased, it will transmit to the organ it overcomes." If left untreated, according to a pattern of three months or six months, or three days or six days, the disease will transmit through all five zang organs and result in death. This is known as transmission following the sequence of overcoming. Hence it is said: "To distinguish between yang conditions, one knows where the disease originates; " to distinguish between yin conditions, one understands the timing of life and death." This means knowing when a disease reaches its fatal stage.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	玉機真藏... :	
是故風者百病之長也，今風寒客於人，使人毫毛畢直，皮膚閉而為熱，當是之時，可汗而發也；或痺不仁腫痛，當是之時，可湯熨及火灸刺而去之。弗治，病入舍於肺，名曰肺痺，發欬上氣。弗治，肺即傳而行之肝，病名曰肝痺，一名曰厥，脇痛出食，當是之時，可按若刺耳。弗治，肝傳之脾，病名曰脾風，發癉，腹中熱，煩心出黃，當此之時，可按可藥可浴。弗治，脾傳之腎，病名曰疝瘕，少腹冤熱而痛，出白，一名曰蠱，當此之時，可按可藥。弗治，腎傳之心，病筋脈相引而急，病名曰瘛，當此之時，可灸可藥。弗治，滿十日，法當死。腎因傳之心，心即復反傳而行之肺，發寒熱，法當三歲死，此病之次也。
		Therefore, wind is the leader of all diseases. When external wind and cold invade a person, they cause the body hairs to stand upright and the skin to close up, resulting in heat. At this stage, it can be treated by inducing perspiration; or if there is numbness without sensation and swelling with pain, at this stage it can be removed by fomentation, moxibustion, or acupuncture. If left untreated, the disease enters and lodges in the lungs, known as lung bi syndrome, causing coughing and upward rushing qi. If left untreated, it will then transmit to the liver. The disease is called liver bi syndrome, also known as jue syndrome, characterized by costal pain and vomiting of food. At this stage, pressing or acupuncture may be used for treatment. If left untreated, the liver will transmit it to the spleen. The disease is named "spleen wind," manifesting as jaundice, internal abdominal heat, mental distress, and yellow discharge. At this stage, treatment may involve pressing, medication, or bathing. If left untreated, the spleen will transmit it to the kidneys. The disease is called "hernia and abdominal mass," characterized by pain in the lower abdomen with a sensation of heat and discharge of white fluid; it is also known as "gu." At this stage, pressing and medication may be used for treatment. If left untreated, the kidneys will transmit it to the heart. The disease causes tension and pulling of tendons and vessels, known as "chidi." At this stage, moxibustion or medication may be used for treatment. If left untreated, within ten days the person will die according to this pattern. The kidneys transmit it to the heart, and then the heart transmits back to the lungs. This causes chills and fever, and according to this pattern, death should occur within three years; this is the sequence of disease transmission.

9
打開字典
	玉機真藏... :	
然其卒發者，不必治於傳，或其傳化有不以次，不以次入者，憂恐悲喜怒，令不得以其次，故令人有大病矣。因而喜大虛則腎氣乘矣，怒則肝氣乘矣，悲則肺氣乘矣，恐則脾氣乘矣，憂則心氣乘矣，此其道也。故病有五，五五二十五變，及其傳化。傳，乘之名也。
		However, in sudden onset cases, the disease does not necessarily follow the transmission sequence. Sometimes it transforms or enters without following the order—emotions such as worry, fear, sorrow, joy, and anger can disrupt this sequence, thus causing serious illness. Therefore, excessive joy greatly depletes the kidneys, allowing kidney qi to be affected; anger harms the liver, allowing liver qi to be affected; sorrow harms the lungs, allowing lung qi to be affected; fear harms the spleen, allowing spleen qi to be affected; and worry harms the heart, allowing heart qi to be affected. This is the principle of it. Therefore, there are five types of diseases, each with five variations, making twenty-five changes in total when considering their transmission and transformation. Transmission is known as "cheng," the name for one organ affecting another.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	玉機真藏... :	
大骨枯槀，大肉陷下，胸中氣滿，喘息不便，其氣動形，期六月死，真藏脈見，乃予之期日。大骨枯槀，大肉陷下，胸中氣滿，喘息不便，內痛引肩項，期一月死，真藏見，乃予之期日。大骨枯槀，大肉陷下，胸中氣滿，喘息不便，內痛引肩項，期一月死，真藏見，乃予之期日。大骨枯槀，大肉陷下，胸中氣滿，喘息不便，內痛引肩項，身熱脫肉破䐃，真藏見，十月之內死。大骨枯槀，大肉陷下，肩髓內消，動作益衰，真藏來見，期一歲死，見其真藏，乃予之期日。大骨枯槀，大肉陷下，胸中氣滿，腹內痛，心中不便，肩項身熱，破䐃脫肉，目匡陷，真藏見，目不見人，立死，其見人者，至其所不勝之時則死。急虛身中卒至，五藏絕閉，脈道不通，氣不往來，譬於墮溺，不可為期。其脈絕不來，若人一息五六至，其形肉不脫，真藏雖不見，猶死也。真肝脈至，中外急，如循刀刃責責然，如按琴瑟弦，色青白不澤，毛折，乃死。真心脈至，堅而搏，如循薏苡子累累然，色赤黑不澤，毛折，乃死。真肺脈至，大而虛，如以毛羽中人膚，色白赤不澤，毛折，乃死。真腎脈至，搏而絕，如指彈石辟辟然，色黑黃不澤，毛折，乃死。真脾脈至，弱而乍數乍踈，色黃青不澤，毛折，乃死。諸真藏脈見者，皆死，不治也。黃帝曰：見真藏曰死，何也。歧伯曰：五藏者，皆稟氣於胃，胃者，五藏之本也，藏氣者，不能自致於手太陰，必因於胃氣，乃至於手太陰也，故五藏各以其時，自為而至於手太陰也。故邪氣勝者，精氣衰也，故病甚者，胃氣不能與之俱至於手太陰，故真藏之氣獨見，獨見者病勝藏也，故曰死。帝曰：善。
		When the large bones are dry and withered, the body mass sinks inward, the chest is filled with qi causing difficulty in breathing, and the movement of qi visibly affects the body's form, death will occur within six months. When the true zang pulse appears, a specific date for death can be given. When the large bones are dried and withered, body mass sinks inward, chest is filled with qi making breathing difficult, internal pain radiates to the shoulders and neck, death will occur within one month. When the true zang pulses appear, a specific date of death can be given. When the large bones are withered and dry, body mass sinks inward; chest is filled with qi, making breathing difficult. Internal pain radiates to shoulders and neck. Death will occur within one month; when true zang pulses appear, then a specific date can be given for death. When the large bones are withered, and body mass sinks inward; when chest is filled with qi and breathing becomes difficult; internal pain radiates to shoulders and back of the neck; when the body feels hot, muscles waste away, and joints break down; when true zang pulses become evident, death will occur within ten months. When the large bones are withering and dry, body mass is sunken inward, marrow within the shoulders deteriorates, movements become increasingly weak; when true zang pulses manifest, death will occur in one year. When these true zang pulses are observed, a specific date of demise can be given. When the large bones are withered dry, body mass sunken inward; chest filled with qi, abdominal pain within, discomfort in the heart region; shoulders, neck and body feel hot; joints break down and muscles waste away; eye sockets sink in. When true zang pulses appear and eyesight is lost to the point of not seeing people, death occurs immediately. If sight remains but only for a short time, then death will occur when it reaches its overcoming element. In cases of sudden severe deficiency, the condition arises abruptly within the body; all five zang functions are cut off and blocked, pulse pathways become obstructed, and qi ceases to circulate. This is like falling into water or drowning—no specific time for death can be predicted. When the pulse is completely absent, or when it beats five to six times in one breath, yet body mass has not wasted away and true zang pulses remain unseen, death will still occur. When the true liver pulse arrives, it is tense both internally and externally, like feeling along a sharp blade with a taut sensation, or pressing on the strings of a qin or se. When the complexion appears blue-white without luster, and hair breaks off, death will occur. When the true heart pulse arrives, it is firm and forceful, like touching a cluster of yiyi seeds. The complexion appears red-black without luster; when hair breaks off, death occurs. When the true lung pulse arrives, it is large yet hollow, like a feather brushing against the skin. The complexion appears white and red without luster; when body hair breaks off, death occurs. When the true kidney pulse arrives, it is forceful yet cut off abruptly, like a finger striking stone with a sharp sound. The complexion appears black and yellow without luster; when body hair falls out, death occurs. When the true spleen pulse arrives, it is weak and fluctuates between rapid and slow. The complexion appears yellow and green without luster; when body hair is shed, death occurs. When any of the true zang pulses are seen, it indicates certain death; no treatment can be effective. The Yellow Emperor asked: "Seeing a true zang pulse means death—why is that?" Qibo said: "The five zang organs all receive their qi from the stomach. The stomach is the root of the five zang. The qi of the zang cannot reach the Taiyin meridian on its own, but must rely upon the stomach's qi to arrive at the Taiyin meridians. Therefore, each of the five zang transmits its condition in accordance with their respective times and reaches the Taiyin through this process." Therefore, when pathogenic qi prevails, it is because the healthy essence qi has declined. In severe diseases, stomach qi cannot accompany the zang qi to reach the Taiyin meridians; thus, only the true zang qi appears. When this happens alone, it indicates that disease has overcome the zang organ, hence death occurs. The Emperor said: "Good."
