打開字典
顯示相似段落
	玉機真藏... :	
黃帝曰：凡治病，察其形氣色澤，脈之盛衰，病之新故，乃治之無後其時。形氣相得，謂之可治；色澤以浮，謂之易已；脈從四時，謂之可治；脈弱以滑，是有胃氣，命曰易治，取之以時。形氣相失，謂之難治；色夭不澤，謂之難已；脈實以堅，謂之益甚；脈逆四時，為不可治。必察四難，而明告之。所謂逆四時者，春得肺脈，夏得腎脈，秋得心脈，冬得脾脈，其至皆懸絕沈濇者，命曰逆四時。未有藏形，於春夏而脈沈濇，秋冬而脈浮大，名曰逆四時也。病熱脈靜，泄而脈大，脫血而脈實，病在中脈實堅，病在外，脈不實堅者，皆難治。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "In treating diseases, one must examine the body form, qi, color and luster of complexion, the strength or decline of pulses, whether the disease is new or chronic. Then treatment should be administered without delaying the appropriate time." When body form and qi are in harmony, it is considered treatable; when the complexion has a superficial luster, it indicates an easy recovery; When the pulse follows the four seasons' patterns, it is considered treatable. A weak yet smooth pulse indicates presence of stomach qi; this condition is termed easily treatable and should be addressed at the appropriate time. When body form and qi are disharmonious, it is considered difficult to treat; when the complexion appears dull without luster, it indicates a condition that is hard to recover from; a firm and solid pulse signifies worsening of the disease; When a pulse contradicts the patterns of the four seasons, it is untreatable. One must carefully examine these four difficulties and clearly inform the patient about them. What is meant by "contradicting the four seasons" is when in spring one obtains a lung pulse, in summer a kidney pulse, in autumn a heart pulse, and in winter a spleen pulse. If all these pulses appear as if suspended, severed, deep, or rough, it is termed contradicting the four seasons. If there are no signs of zang organ conditions yet, but in spring and summer the pulse is deep and rough, while in autumn and winter it is floating and large, this is also called contradicting the four seasons. In cases of heat illness with a static pulse, diarrhea with a large pulse, blood loss with a solid pulse, internal disease with a solid and firm pulse, or external disease where the pulse is not solid or firm—these are all difficult to treat.

12
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	玉機真藏... :	
黃帝曰：余聞虛實以決死生，願聞其情。歧伯曰：五實死，五虛死。帝曰：願聞五實五虛。歧伯曰：脈盛，皮熱，腹脹，前後不通，悶瞀，此謂五實。脈細，皮寒，氣少，泄利前後，飲食不入，此謂五虛。帝曰：其時有生者，何也。歧伯曰：漿粥入胃，泄注止，則虛者活；身汗得後利，則實者活。此其候也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "I have heard that deficiency and excess determine life and death; I wish to hear the details." Qibo said: "Five types of excess lead to death, and five types of deficiency also lead to death." The Emperor said: "I wish to hear about the Five Excesses and the Five Deficiencies." Qibo said: "A strong pulse, hot skin, abdominal distension, obstruction of urination and defecation, and mental confusion—these are called the Five Excesses." A weak pulse, cold skin, shallow breathing, diarrhea with frequent urination and defecation, inability to take in food or drink—these are called the Five Deficiencies. The Emperor asked: "Yet there are cases where people survive despite these conditions; why is that?" Qibo said: "When porridge and soup enter the stomach, diarrhea stops, then those with deficiency may survive; when body perspiration occurs and bowels are cleared, those with excess conditions may also survive." These are the signs to observe. 

20. 三部九候論 Three Sections and Nine Pulse Manifestations Treatise

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	三部九候... :	
黃帝問曰：余聞九鍼於夫子，眾多博大，不可勝數。余願聞要道，以屬子孫，傳之後世，著之骨髓，藏之肝肺，歃血而受，不敢妄泄，令合天道，必有終始，上應天光星辰歷紀，下副四時五行，貴賤更互，冬陰夏陽，以人應之奈何，願聞其方。歧伯對曰：妙乎哉問也！此天地之至數。帝曰：願聞天地之至數，合於人形，血氣通，決死生，為之奈何。歧伯曰：天地之至數，始於一，終於九焉。一者天，二者地，三者人，因而三之，三三者九，以應九野。故人有三部，部有三候，以決死生，以處百病，以調虛實，而除邪疾。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I have heard from my teacher about the Nine Needles, which are numerous and extensive beyond count. I wish to hear the essential principles, so that I may pass them on to my descendants and transmit them to future generations. Let these teachings be deeply ingrained in bone and marrow, hidden within liver and lungs, accepted with a blood oath, never carelessly revealed. Thus they will conform to the laws of Heaven, necessarily possessing beginning and end; above corresponding to celestial light, stars, constellations, and chronological records; below harmonizing with the four seasons and Five Elements. The noble and humble alternate in turn; winter is yin while summer is yang. How should a human being correspond to these? I wish to hear your method. Qibo replied: What an excellent question! This is the most profound principle of Heaven and Earth. The Emperor said: I wish to hear about this supreme principle of Heaven and Earth, how it corresponds with the human form, connects blood and qi, determines life or death, and what should be done in response. Qibo said: The supreme principle of Heaven and Earth begins with one and ends at nine. One represents Heaven, two represents Earth, three represents humanity; thus multiplying by three, thrice three makes nine, corresponding to the Nine Wilds. Therefore, a person has three sections, each section having three pulses; these are used to determine life or death, diagnose all kinds of illnesses, regulate deficiency and excess, and eliminate pathogenic influences.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	三部九候... :	
帝曰：何謂三部。歧伯曰：有下部，有中部，有上部，部各有三候，三候者，有天有地有人也，必指而導之，乃以為真。上部天，兩額之動脈；上部地，兩額之動脈；上部人，耳前之動脈。中部天，手太陰也；中部地，手陽明也；中部人，手少陰也。下部天，足厥陰也；下部地，足少陰也；下部人，足太陰也。故下部之天以候肝，地以候腎，人以候脾胃之氣。帝曰：中部之候奈何。歧伯曰：亦有天，亦有地，亦有人。天以候肺，地以候胸中之氣，人以候心。帝曰：上部以何候之。歧伯曰：亦有天，亦有地，亦有人，天以候頭角之氣，地以候口齒之氣，人以候耳目之氣。三部者，各有天，各有地，各有人。三而成天，三而成地，三而成人，三而三之，合則為九，九分為九野，九野為九藏。故神藏五，形藏四，合為九藏。五藏已敗，其色必夭，夭必死矣。帝曰：以候奈何。歧伯曰：必先度其形之肥瘦，以調其氣之虛實，實則寫之，虛則補之。必先去其血脈而後調之，無問其病，以平為期。
		The Emperor said: What is meant by the Three Sections? Qibo said: There is the lower section, the middle section, and the upper section. Each section has three pulses. These three pulses correspond to Heaven, Earth, and Humanity; one must point them out and guide accordingly in order to discern what is true. The upper section's Heaven pulse is the pulsation at both eyebrows. The upper section's Earth pulse is also the pulsation at both brows; the upper section's Humanity pulse is the pulsation in front of the ears. The middle section's Heaven pulse corresponds to the Taiyin meridian of the hand; the middle section's Earth pulse corresponds to the Yangming meridian of the hand; 中部人，手少陰也。 the middle section's Humanity pulse corresponds to the Shaoyin meridian of the hand. The lower section's Heaven pulse corresponds to Jueyin of the foot; the lower section's Earth pulse corresponds to Shaoyin of the foot; 下部人，足太陰也。 the lower section's Humanity pulse corresponds to Taiyin of the foot. Therefore, the Heaven pulse in the lower section is used to observe the liver; the Earth pulse observes the kidneys; and the Humanity pulse observes the qi of the spleen and stomach. The Emperor said: What about the pulses in the middle section? Qibo said: It also has Heaven, Earth, and Humanity. The Heaven pulse observes the lungs; the Earth pulse observes qi in the chest cavity; the Humanity pulse observes the heart. The Emperor said: What do the pulses of the upper section observe? Qibo said: It also has Heaven, Earth and Humanity. The Heaven pulse observes the qi of head and temples; the Earth pulse observes the qi of mouth and teeth; the Humanity pulse observes the qi in ears and eyes. The Three Sections each have their Heaven, their Earth, and their Humanity. Three form Heaven, three form Earth, three form Humanity; combining the threes thrice over makes nine. Divided into nine parts, these correspond to the Nine Wilds, and the Nine Wilds relate to the Nine Zang (viscera). Therefore, five are spiritual viscera, four are physical viscera; together they make nine viscera. When the Five Viscera have deteriorated, their complexion will certainly appear withered; when withering appears, death is inevitable. The Emperor said: How should one observe these? Qibo said: One must first assess the person's body type, whether fat or thin, to regulate their qi in terms of deficiency and excess. When there is excess, it should be drained; when there is deficiency, it should be supplemented. One must first remove the blood and vessels' obstructions before regulating them. Regardless of the illness, one should aim for balance as the goal.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	三部九候... :	
帝曰：決死生奈何。歧伯曰：形盛脈細，少氣不足以息者，危。形瘦脈大，胸中多氣者，死。形氣相得者，生。參伍不調者，病。三部九候皆相失者，死。上下左右之脈相應如參舂者，病甚。上下左右相失不可數者，死。中部之候雖獨調，與眾藏相失者，死。中部之候相減者，死。目內陷者死。帝曰：何以知病之所在。歧伯曰：察九候獨小者，病，獨大者，病，獨疾者，病，獨遲者，病，獨熱者，病，獨寒者，病，獨陷下者，病。以左手足上，上去踝五寸按之，庶右手足當踝而彈之，其應過五寸以上，蠕蠕然者，不病；其應疾，中手渾渾然者，病；中手徐徐然者，病；其應上不能至五寸，彈之不應者，死。是以脫肉身不去者，死。中部乍踈乍數者，死。其脈代而鉤者，病在絡脈。九候之相應也，上下若一，不得相失。一候後則病，二候後則病甚，三候後則病危。所謂後者，應不俱也。察其府藏，以知死生之期。
		The Emperor said: How can one determine life or death? Qibo said: When the body is strong but the pulse is fine, and there is little qi insufficient for breathing, it indicates danger. When the body is thin but the pulse is large, and there is excessive qi in the chest, death will occur. When form and qi harmonize with each other, life is sustained. When the three pulses are inconsistent or disharmonious, it indicates illness. When all three sections and their nine pulses are completely out of harmony with each other, death will occur. When the pulses above, below, left, and right correspond to one another like a pestle in motion, it indicates severe illness. When the pulses above, below, to the left, and to the right are completely disordered and cannot be counted or discerned, death will occur. Even if the pulses of the middle section appear harmonious on their own, but are completely discordant with those of other viscera, death will occur. When the pulses in the middle section gradually weaken or diminish, it indicates impending death. Those whose eyes sink inward will die. The Emperor said: How can one know the location of a disease? Qibo said: When examining the Nine Pulses, if one is uniquely small, it indicates disease; if uniquely large, it indicates disease; uniquely rapid, it indicates disease; unusually slow, it indicates disease; abnormally hot, it indicates disease; excessively cold, it indicates disease; or uniquely sunken and weak, it also indicates disease. Pressing with the left hand five cun above the ankle on the left foot and feeling it, while tapping at the corresponding point over the right ankle with the right hand; if the response extends more than five cun upward in a gentle, quivering manner, then there is no illness. If the response is rapid and feels heavy or muddled upon contact with the hand, it indicates disease; if the response is slow and sluggish when touched by the hand, it also indicates illness; If the response does not reach five cun upward and there is no reaction to tapping, death will occur. Therefore, when the body has lost flesh but the person remains bedridden without recovery, it indicates death. When the middle section's pulse is suddenly irregular and alternates between slow and rapid, it signifies impending death. If the pulse is intermittent (dai) yet also hooked (gou), the illness lies in the collateral vessels. The correspondence among the Nine Pulses should be harmonious, with those above and below being unified; they must not become discordant or lose their coherence. If one pulse lags behind the others, illness occurs; if two pulses lag, the illness is severe; if all three pulses lag, the condition becomes critical. What is meant by "lagging" (hou) is that the pulse does not arrive in harmony with the others. Examine their fu-zang (organs and viscera) to determine the timing of life or death.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	三部九候... :	
必先知經脈，然後知病脈，真藏脈見者，勝死。足太陽氣絕者，其足不可屈伸，死必戴眼。帝曰：冬陰夏陽奈何。歧伯曰：九候之脈，皆沈細懸絕者為陰，主冬，故以夜半死。盛躁喘數者為陽，主夏，故以日中死。是故寒熱病者，以平旦死。熱中及熱病者，以日中死。病風者，以日夕死。病水者，以夜半死。其脈乍踈乍數乍遲乍疾者，日乘四季死。形肉已脫，九候雖調，猶死。七診雖見，九候皆從者不死。所言不死者，風氣之病，及經月之病，似七診之病，而非也，故言不死。若有七診之病，其脈候亦敗者，死矣，必發噦噫。必審問其所始病，與今之所方病，而後各切循其脈，視其經絡浮沈，以上下逆從循之，其脈疾者不病，其脈遲者病，脈不往來者，死，皮膚著者，死。
		One must first understand the meridians before one can recognize pathological pulses; when the true zang (visceral) pulse appears, it indicates a victory over death. When the qi of the Taiyang meridian in the foot is cut off, the feet cannot bend or stretch; death will certainly occur with the eyes rolling upward. The Emperor said: How should one understand winter as yin and summer as yang? Qibo said: When the pulses of all nine sections are deep, fine, and suspended or cut off, it is yin in nature, governing winter; thus death occurs at midnight. When the pulse is strong, restless, and rapid with panting, it belongs to yang, governing summer; therefore, death occurs at noon. Therefore, those suffering from cold or heat illnesses will die at dawn. Those with internal heat and acute febrile diseases will die at noon. Those suffering from wind-related illnesses will die in the evening. Those with water-related diseases will die at midnight. When the pulse alternates between irregular, rapid, slow, and fast, death occurs on days corresponding to the four seasons' transitions. If the body's flesh has already wasted away, even if the nine pulses appear harmonious, death will still occur. Even if seven diagnostic signs are evident, as long as all nine pulses remain in harmony, death will not occur. What is referred to as "not dying" includes diseases caused by wind or those lasting for months, which resemble the seven diagnostic signs of illness but are not truly so; therefore, it is said that death will not occur. If a person exhibits the seven diagnostic signs of illness and their pulse condition has also deteriorated, then death is certain; they will inevitably experience hiccups and belching. One must carefully inquire into the initial onset of illness and the current symptoms, then palpate each pulse in detail. Examine their meridians and collaterals to determine whether they are superficial or deep, moving upward or downward, following or resisting normal flow. If the pulse is rapid but there is no disease, it indicates health; if slow, it suggests illness. A pulse that does not ebb and flow signifies death. Skin that adheres tightly without elasticity also indicates death.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	三部九候... :	
帝曰：其可治者奈何。歧伯曰：經病者治其經，孫絡病者治其孫絡血，血病身有痛者，治其經絡。其病者在奇邪，奇邪之脈則繆刺之。留瘦不移，節而刺之。上實下虛，切而從之，索其結絡脈，刺出其血，以見通之。瞳子高者，太陽不足，戴眼者，太陽已絕，此決死生之要，不可不察也。手指及手外踝上五指，留鍼。
		The Emperor said: What about those who can be treated? Qibo said: For diseases of the meridians, treat the meridians; for diseases in the collateral vessels (sun luo), remove blood from the collateral vessels; if there is pain due to a blood disorder, treat the meridians and collaterals. If the illness arises from abnormal pathogenic influences (qi xie), then for pulses affected by such irregularities, use the method of miao ci (misperforation acupuncture). If the condition is stagnant and unchanging with wasting, treat it in a segmented manner at intervals. When there is excess above and deficiency below, palpate carefully and follow the condition; locate the knotted collaterals and vessels, needle them to release blood, thereby restoring unimpeded flow. Those with elevated pupils indicate insufficiency of the Taiyang; those whose eyes roll upward (taiyan) indicate that the Taiyang has been cut off. These are essential signs for determining life or death and must not be overlooked. Retain needles at the fingers and five points above the outer ankle of the hand. 

21. 經脈別論 Treatise on Differentiating Meridians

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	經脈別論:	
黃帝問曰：人之居處動靜勇怯，脈亦為之變乎。歧伯對曰：凡人之驚恐恚勞動靜，皆為變也。是以夜行則喘出於腎，淫氣病肺。有所墮恐，喘出於肝，淫氣害脾。有所驚恐，喘出於肺，淫氣傷心。度水跌仆，喘出於腎與骨，當是之時，勇者氣行則已，怯者則著而為病也。故曰：診病之道，觀人勇怯骨肉皮膚，能知其情，以為診法也。故飲食飽甚，汗出於胃。驚而奪精，汗出於心。持重遠行，汗出於腎。疾走恐懼，汗出於肝。搖體勞苦，汗出於脾。故春秋冬夏，四時陰陽，生病起於過用，此為常也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: When people live and dwell, move or remain still, are courageous or timid, does the pulse also change accordingly? Qibo replied: Whenever people experience startlement, fear, anger, toil, movement or stillness, these all constitute changes. Therefore, when one walks at night, breath is emitted from the kidneys, and excessive qi harms the lungs. When someone falls into fear or alarm, breath is emitted from the liver, and excessive qi injures the spleen. When someone experiences startlement or terror, breath is emitted from the lungs, and excessive qi damages the heart. When one crosses water or falls down, breath is emitted from the kidney and bones. At such a time, the courageous have their qi flow normally and recover, while the timid have their qi stagnate and become illness. Therefore it is said: The method of diagnosing disease involves observing a person's courage or timidity, the bones, flesh and skin, so as to understand their condition, which forms the basis for diagnostic methods. Therefore, when one eats and drinks excessively, perspiration emerges from the stomach. łoż When startled to the point of losing essence, perspiration arises from the heart. Carrying heavy loads and traveling far, perspiration emerges from the kidneys. Rushing about in fear, perspiration arises from the liver. Shaking the body and toiling laboriously, perspiration emerges from the spleen. Therefore, in spring, summer, autumn, and winter, during the four seasons with their yin and yang changes, diseases arise from excessive exertion; this is a common occurrence.

2
打開字典
	經脈別論:	
食氣入胃，散精於肝，淫氣於筋。淫氣入胃，濁氣歸心，淫精於脈。脈氣流經，經氣歸於肺，肺朝百脈，輸精於皮毛。毛脈合精，行氣於腑。腑精神明，留於四臟，氣歸於椎衡。權衡以平，氣口成寸，以決死生。飲入於胃，遊溢精氣，上輸於脾。脾氣散精，上歸於肺，通調水道，下輸膀胱。水精四布，五經並行，合於四時五臟陰陽，揆度以為常也。
		Food qi enters the stomach, distributes essence to the liver, and spreads qi to the sinews. Excessive qi entering the stomach causes turbid qi to return to the heart, and excessive essence flows into the vessels. Vessel qi circulates through the meridians; meridian qi returns to the lungs. The lungs receive the hundred vessels, and transport essence to the skin and hair. The hair and vessel combine essence, circulating qi through the fu organs. The fu organs' spirit and energy become clear, remaining in the four zang organs; qi returns to the vertebrae and balance. When the scales are balanced evenly, the qigu kou forms a cun measurement, which is used to determine life or death. Drink enters the stomach; it circulates refined qi upward and transports it to the spleen. The spleen distributes essence, sending it upward to the lungs; it regulates water passages downward into the bladder. Water and essence spread throughout four directions, with five meridians operating concurrently; they harmonize with the four seasons, five zang organs, yin and yang. This is measured and estimated as a constant pattern.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	經脈別論:	
太陽藏獨至，厥喘虛氣逆，是陰不足陽有餘也，表裏當俱寫，取之下俞。陽明藏獨至，是陽氣重并也，當寫陽補陰，取之下俞。少陽藏獨至，是厥氣也，蹻前卒大，取之下俞，少陽獨至者，一陽之過也。太陰藏搏者，用心省真，五脈氣少，胃氣不平，三陰也，宜治其下俞，補陽寫陰。一陽獨嘯，少陽厥也，陽并於上，四脈爭張，氣歸於腎，宜治其經絡，寫陽補陰。一陰至，厥陰之治也，真虛㾓心，厥氣留薄，發為白汗，調食和藥，治在下俞。
		When the taiyang organ's qi arrives alone, it causes coldness, panting, deficiency of qi and reversal; this indicates yin insufficiency and excess yang. Both exterior and interior should be purged, by taking points at the lower shu (transport) points. When the yangming organ's qi arrives alone, this indicates excessive accumulation of yang energy; one should purge yang and tonify yin, by taking points at the lower Shu (transport) points. When the shaoyang organ's qi arrives alone, it is a manifestation of reversal qi; there will be sudden swelling in front of the jiao vessels. Take points at the lower Shu to treat this condition. When shaoyang arrives alone, it indicates an excess of one yang energy. When the taiyin organ's pulse is forceful, one should use the mind to examine its authenticity. When five pulses have weak qi and stomach qi is unbalanced, it belongs to three yins; treatment should target the lower Shu points, tonifying yang and purging yin. When one yang alone is dominant, it indicates a reversal of shaoyang; yang accumulates upward, the four pulses contend and expand, qi returns to the kidney. Treatment should target its meridians and collaterals, purging yang and tonifying yin. When one yin arrives, it is the manifestation of jueyin; true deficiency affects the heart, reversal qi lingers and invades superficially, manifesting as cold sweat. Adjust diet and harmonize medicine; treatment should focus on lower Shu points.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	經脈別論:	
帝曰：太陽藏何象。歧伯曰：象三陽而浮也。帝曰：少陽藏何象。歧伯曰：象一陽也，一陽藏者，滑而不實也。帝曰：陽明藏何象。歧伯曰：象大浮也，太陰藏搏，言伏鼓也。二陰搏至，腎沈不浮也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: What is the manifestation of the taiyang organ? Qibo said: It manifests as three yang with a floating quality. The Yellow Emperor asked: What is the manifestation of the shaoyang organ? Qibo said: It manifests as one yang. When one yang dominates, the pulse is slippery but not solid. The Yellow Emperor asked: What is the manifestation of the yangming organ? lodash Qibo said: It manifests as large and floating. When taiyin's pulse is forceful, it means a suppressed drum-like quality. When two yins are forceful in the pulse, it indicates that kidney qi sinks and does not float. 

22. 藏氣法時論 Treatise on the Regulation of Zang Organ Qi According to Seasonal Laws

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
相關討論
	藏氣法時... :	
黃帝問曰：合人形以法四時五行而治，何如而從，何如而逆，得失之意，願聞其事。歧伯對曰：五行者，金木水火土也，更貴更賤，以知死生，以決成敗，而定五藏之氣，間甚之時，死生之期也。帝曰：願卒聞之。歧伯曰：肝主春，足厥陰少陽主治，其日甲乙，肝苦急，急食甘以緩之。心主夏，手少陰太陽主治，其日丙丁，心苦緩，急食酸以收之。脾主長夏，足太陰陽明主治，其日戊己，脾苦濕，急食甘以緩之。心主夏，手少陰太陽主治，其日丙丁，心苦緩，急食酸以收之。脾主長夏，足太陰陽明主治，其日戊己，脾苦濕，急食苦以燥之。肺主秋，手太陰陽明主治，其日庚辛，肺苦氣上逆，急食苦以泄之。腎主冬，足少陰太陽主治，其日壬癸，腎苦燥，急食辛以潤之，開腠理，致津液，通氣也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: If one treats the human body according to the principles of the four seasons and the five elements, under what circumstances is this in harmony (from), and under what circumstances is it contrary (逆)? I wish to understand the meaning of success and failure. Please tell me about these matters. Qibo replied: The five elements are metal, wood, water, fire, and earth. They alternate in dominance and decline; by understanding this, one can know life and death, decide success or failure, determine the qi of the five zang organs, discern the severity of intervals, and thus establish the time for life or death. The Emperor said: I wish to hear it in full. Qibo said: The liver governs spring, and the foot Jueyin Shaoyang channels are in charge of regulation. Their corresponding days are Jia and Yi. The liver dislikes tension; to relieve it, one should consume sweet foods to ease its condition. The heart governs summer, and the hand Shaoyin Taiyang channels are in charge of regulation. The corresponding days are Bing and Ding. The heart dislikes relaxation; to correct this, one should urgently consume sour foods to consolidate it. The spleen governs the late summer, and the foot Taiyin Yangming channels are in charge of regulation. Its corresponding days are Wu and Ji. The spleen dislikes dampness; to relieve this, one should urgently consume sweet foods to ease it. The heart governs summer; the hand Shaoyin and Taiyang channels are responsible for regulation. The corresponding days are Bing (丙) and Ding (丁). The heart dislikes relaxation, so one should urgently consume sour food to consolidate it. The spleen governs late summer; the foot Taiyin and Yangming channels are responsible for regulation. Its corresponding days are Wu (戊) and Ji (己). The spleen dislikes dampness, so one should urgently consume bitter food to dry it out. The lungs govern autumn; the hand Taiyin and Yangming channels regulate it. Its corresponding days are Geng (庚) and Xin (辛). The lungs dislike upward reversal of qi, so one should urgently consume bitter foods to purge it. The kidneys govern winter; the foot Shaoyin and Taiyang channels regulate it. The corresponding days are Ren (壬) and Gui (癸). The kidneys dislike dryness, so one should urgently consume pungent foods to moisten them, open the pores, promote body fluids, and facilitate the flow of qi.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	藏氣法時... :	
病在肝，愈於夏，夏不愈，甚於秋，秋不死，持於冬，起於春，禁當風。肝病者，愈在丙丁，丙丁不愈，加於庚辛，庚辛不死，持於壬癸，起於甲乙。肝病者，平旦慧，下晡甚，夜半靜。肝欲散，急食辛以散之，用辛補之，酸寫之。
		When illness is in the liver, it will recover during summer; if not recovered by summer, it will worsen in autumn. If one survives through autumn, the condition will stabilize in winter and begin to improve in spring. One should avoid exposure to wind. Those with liver disease will recover on the days Bing and Ding; if not recovered on these days, it will worsen on Geng and Xin. If one survives past Geng and Xin, the condition will stabilize on Ren and Gui, and begin to improve on Jia and Yi. Those with liver disease are lucid at dawn, worsen in the late afternoon, and become calm by midnight. The liver prefers dispersion; to promote this, one should urgently consume pungent substances to disperse it. Pungent flavors are used for tonification, and sour flavors for elimination.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	藏氣法時... :	
病在心，愈在長夏，長夏不愈，甚於冬，冬不死，持於春，起於夏，禁溫食熱衣。心病者，愈在戊己，戊己不愈，加於壬癸，壬癸不死，持於甲乙，起於丙丁。心病者，日中慧，夜半甚，平旦靜。心欲耎，急食鹹以耎之，用鹹補之，甘寫之。
		When illness is in the heart, it will recover during late summer; if not recovered then, it will worsen during winter. If one survives through winter, the condition will stabilize during spring and begin to improve during summer. One should avoid warm foods and hot clothing. Those with heart disease will recover on the days Wu and Ji; if not recovered then, the condition worsens on Ren and Gui. If one survives past Ren and Gui, it stabilizes on Jia and Yi, and begins to improve on Bing and Ding. Those with heart disease are lucid at noon, worsen by midnight, and become calm at dawn. The heart prefers softness; to achieve this, one should urgently consume salty substances to soften it. Salty flavors are used for tonification and sweet flavors for elimination.

病在脾，愈在秋，秋不愈，甚於春，春不死，持於夏，起於長夏，禁溫食飽食濕地濡衣。脾病者，愈在庚辛，庚辛不愈，加於甲乙，甲乙不死，持於丙丁，起於戊己。脾病者，日昳慧，日出甚，下晡靜。脾欲緩，急食甘以緩之，用苦寫之，甘補之。
		When illness is in the spleen, it will recover during autumn; if not recovered by autumn, it will worsen in spring. If one survives through spring, the condition stabilizes in summer and begins to improve in late summer. One should avoid warm food, overeating, damp ground, or wet clothing. Those with spleen disease will recover on the days Geng and Xin; if not recovered then, their condition worsens on Jia and Yi. If they survive past Jia and Yi, it stabilizes on Bing and Ding, and begins to improve on Wu and Ji. Those with spleen disease are lucid in the afternoon, worsen at sunrise, and become calm in the late afternoon. The spleen prefers relaxation; to achieve this, one should consume sweet substances urgently to ease it. Bitter flavors are used for elimination and sweet flavors for tonification.

If one survives past Wu and Ji, the condition stabilizes on Geng and Xin and begins to improve on Ren and Gui. Those with kidney disease are lucid at midnight, worsen during the four seasons' transitional periods, and become calm in late afternoon. The kidneys prefer firmness; to achieve this, one must urgently consume bitter substances to strengthen them. Bitter flavors are used for tonification whereas salty ones are used for elimination.

	藏氣法時... :	
夫邪氣之客於身也，以勝相加，至其所生而愈，至其所不勝而甚，至於所生而持，自得其位而起。必先定五藏之脈，乃可言間甚之時，死生之期也。
		When pathogenic qi lodges in the body, it acts according to the principle of mutual overcoming. The condition improves when it reaches its generating element, worsens when it reaches the element it cannot overcome, stabilizes when it reaches its generated element, and begins to recover when it returns to its proper position. One must first determine the pulses of the five zang organs before one can discuss the timing of severity and the time of life or death.

When deficient, their vision becomes dim or completely lost; they cannot hear well, and often feel fearful as if someone is about to arrest them. To treat this condition, one should take points along its meridians—the Jueyin and Shaoyang channels. If qi rises upward in reverse, it causes headache, deafness, impaired hearing, and swelling of the cheeks. Treat by blood-letting.

They also feel pain along both inner arms. When deficient, they develop a swollen chest and abdomen, with pain drawing downward from beneath the hypochondria to the waist. To treat this condition, take points on its meridians—Shaoyin and Taiyang—and perform blood-letting under the tongue. For any changes or complications in the disease, acupuncture with blood-letting at the 郗 points is indicated.

	藏氣法時... :

		The liver corresponds to the color green; it benefits from sweet flavors. Glutinous rice, beef, jujubes, and chrysanthemum greens are all sweet in nature. The heart corresponds to the color red; it benefits from sour flavors. Adzuki beans, dog meat, plums, and leeks are all sour in nature. The lungs correspond to the color white; they benefit from bitter flavors. Wheat, mutton, apricots, and leeks are all bitter in nature. The spleen corresponds to the color yellow; it benefits from salty flavors. Soybeans, pork, chestnuts, and legume sprouts are all salty in nature. The kidneys correspond to the color black; they benefit from pungent flavors. Yellow millet, chicken meat, peaches, and scallions are all pungent in nature. Pungent disperses; sour consolidates; sweet relaxes; bitter strengthens; salty softens. Medicinal herbs attack pathogenic factors; the five grains provide nourishment, the five fruits offer assistance, the five domestic animals contribute benefit, and the five vegetables serve as supplements. When flavors and properties are combined properly for consumption, they can replenish essence and strengthen qi. These five categories. Each of the five flavors—pungent, sour, sweet, bitter, and salty—has its own specific benefits. They either disperse or consolidate, relax or tighten, strengthen or soften. According to the four seasons and the functions of the five zang organs, diseases follow the suitability of these five flavors. 

23. 宣明五氣 Xuanming Wuqi

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	宣明五氣:	
味所入；酸入肝，辛入肺，苦入心，鹹入腎，甘入脾，是謂五入。
		The flavor that enters; Acid enters the liver, pungent enters the lungs, bitter enters the heart, salty enters the kidneys, and sweet enters the spleen. This is called the five entries.

		The illnesses caused by qi: the heart causes belching, the lungs cause coughing, the liver causes talking, the spleen causes swallowing, the kidneys cause yawning and sneezing, the stomach causes upward rushing of qi, hiccups, and fear, the large intestine and small intestine cause diarrhea, excessive flow from the lower energizer causes water retention, the bladder's obstruction causes difficulty in urination, lack of control leads to involuntary urination, and the gallbladder causes anger. This is called the Five Illnesses.

		The conditions caused by essence: when vital essence converges in the heart, it causes joy; when it converges in the lungs, it causes sorrow; when it converges in the liver, it causes worry; when it converges in the spleen, it causes fear; and when it converges in the kidneys, it causes terror. This is called the Five Convergences, which occur due to deficiency and mutual convergence.

The illness tends to cause anger and cannot be treated. This is referred to as the Five Pathogens, all of which share a fatal prognosis and are untreatable.

24. 血氣形志 Blood and Qi: Form and Spirit

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	血氣形志:	
夫人之常數，太陽常多血少氣，少陽常少血多氣，陽明常多氣多血，少陰常少血多氣，厥陰常多血少氣，太陰常多氣少血，此天之常數。足太陽與少陰為表裏，少陽與厥陰為表裏，陽明與太陰為表裏，是為足陰陽也。手太陽與少陰為表裏，少陽與心主為表裏，陽明與太陰為表裏，是為手之陰陽也。今知手足陰陽所苦，凡治病必先去其血，乃去其所苦，伺之所欲，然後寫有餘，補不足。
		The usual pattern for the human body is that Taiyang usually has more blood and less qi, Shaoyang usually has less blood but more qi, Yangming usually has both more qi and more blood, Shaoyin usually has less blood and more qi, Jueyin usually has more blood but less qi, and Taiyin usually has more qi but less blood. This is the usual pattern of Heaven. The foot taiyang and shaoyin are exterior-interior pairs, the shaoyang and jueyin are exterior-interior pairs, and the yangming and taiyin are exterior-interior pairs; these are known as the foot yin-yang meridians. The hand taiyang and shaoyin form an exterior-interior pair, the shaoyang and xinshu (Heart Protector) form an exterior-interior pair, and the yangming and taiying form an exterior-interior pair; these are known as the hand yin-yang meridians . Now, knowing the ailments of the yin-yang channels in the hands and feet, whenever treating a disease one must first remove the blood, then eliminate what causes suffering, observe what is desired, and only after that drain the excess and supplement the deficiency.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	血氣形志:	
欲知背俞，先度其兩乳間，中折之，更以他草度去半已，即以兩隅相拄也，乃舉以度其背，令其一隅居上，齊脊大椎，兩隅在下，當其下隅者，肺之俞也。得下一度，心之俞也。復下一度，左角肝之俞也，右角脾之俞也。復下一度，腎之俞也。是謂五藏之俞，灸刺之度也。
		To determine the back shu points, first measure between the two nipples. Fold this measurement in half and use another grass to mark off half of it; then hold the two ends together. Use this as a measuring tool for the back, placing one end at the top aligned with the large vertebra (Dazhui) along the spine, while the other two corners are positioned below. The point directly under the lower corner is the shu point of the lung. One measurement downward from this point is the shu point for the heart. Another measurement downward, the left corner corresponds to the liver's shu point and the right corner corresponds to the spleen's shu point. Another measurement downward is the kidney's shu point. These are known as the five zang organs' shu points, and they serve as the standard for acupuncture and moxibustion treatments.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	血氣形志:	
形樂志苦，病生於脈，治之以灸刺。形樂志樂，病生於肉，治之以鍼石。形苦志樂，病生於筋，治之以熨引。形苦志苦，病生於咽嗌，治之以百藥。形數驚恐，經絡不通，病生於不仁，治之以按摩醪藥。是謂五形志也。
		When a person's physical form is at ease but their spirit suffers distress, diseases arise from the meridians; such conditions should be treated with acupuncture and moxibustion. When a person's physical form and spirit are both at ease, diseases arise from the muscles; such conditions should be treated with needles and stones (acupuncture and moxibustion). When a person's body suffers hardship but their spirit is at ease, diseases arise in the tendons; such conditions should be treated by fomentation and stretching. When a person's body and spirit both suffer hardship, diseases arise in the throat and pharynx; such conditions should be treated using various medicines. When a person frequently experiences shock or fear, the meridians and collaterals become obstructed; diseases arise as numbness without sensation. Such conditions should be treated with massage and medicinal decoctions. This is known as the five forms of spirit (or mental states).

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	血氣形志:	
刺陽明出血氣，刺太陽，出血惡氣，刺少陽，出氣惡血，刺太陰，出氣惡血，刺少陰，出氣惡血，刺厥陰，出血惡氣也。
		Puncturing the yangming channel releases both blood and qi; puncturing the taiyang channel releases blood but is harmful to qi; puncturing the shaoyang channel releases qi but is harmful to blood; puncturing the taiyin channel releases qi but is harmful to 血; puncturing the shaoyin channel releases qi but harms blood; and puncturing the jueyin channel releases blood but harms qi. 

25. 寶命全形論 Bao Ming Quan Xing Lun

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	寶命全形... :	
黃帝問曰：天覆地載，萬物悉備，莫貴於人，人以天地之氣生，四時之法成，君王眾庶，盡欲全形，形之疾病，莫知其情，留淫日深，著於骨髓，心私慮之。余欲鍼除其疾病，為之奈何。歧伯對曰：夫鹽之味鹹者，其氣令器津泄；絃絕者，其音嘶敗；木敷者，其葉發；病深者，其聲噦。人有此三者，是謂壞府，毒藥無治，短鍼無取，此皆絕皮傷肉，血氣爭黑。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: Heaven covers and Earth supports, all things are fully provided; nothing is more precious than human beings. Humans receive their life from the qi of Heaven and Earth, and complete themselves according to the laws of the four seasons. Kings, officials, and common people alike all desire to preserve their forms. However, when diseases afflict the body, few understand their true nature. If left untreated, these ailments gradually deepen and settle into the bones and marrow; this is a matter I personally ponder. I wish to use acupuncture to remove their diseases. How should I proceed? Qibo replied: Salt with a salty taste causes the qi of vessels to become moist and leak, due to its nature. When a string is broken, its sound becomes hoarse and deteriorated; When wood flourishes, its leaves sprout; When a disease is deep-seated, the voice becomes hoarse. When a person exhibits these three conditions, it is called the destruction of the zang-fu organs. No toxic medicine can cure such a condition, and even short needles are ineffective. In all these cases, the disease has already penetrated through the skin and damaged the flesh, causing the blood and qi to become darkened in conflict.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	寶命全形... :	
帝曰：余念其痛，心為之亂惑，反甚，其病，不可更代，百姓聞之，以為殘賊，為之柰何。歧伯曰：夫人生於地，懸命於天，天地合氣，命之曰人。人能應四時者，天地為之父母；知萬物者，謂之天子。天有陰陽，人有十二節；天有寒暑，人有虛實。能經天地陰陽之化者，不失四時；知十二節之理者，聖智不能欺也；能存八動之變，五勝更立；能達虛實之數者，獨出獨入，呿吟至微，秋毫在目。
		The Emperor said: I feel compassion for their pain, and my mind becomes confused and agitated. If the disease is treated improperly, it may become worse. Such a condition cannot be easily replaced or reversed. When the common people hear of this, they will regard it as cruel and harmful. How should we deal with this? Qibo said: Humans are born from the Earth, yet their fate is suspended in Heaven. When the qi of Heaven and Earth combine, it is called a human being. Those who can harmonize with the four seasons have Heaven and Earth as their parents; Those who understand all things are called Son of Heaven. Heaven has yin and yang; humans have twelve joints; Heaven has cold and heat; humans experience deficiency and excess. Those who can follow the transformations of Heaven's yin and yang do not lose harmony with the four seasons; Those who understand the principles of the twelve joints are beyond deception, even by sages and wise men; Those who can preserve awareness of the eight movements' changes will witness the five victories taking turns to prevail; Those who comprehend the numerical patterns of deficiency and excess can move freely, entering alone or leaving alone. They perceive even the subtlest sounds and sights—so fine as to see an autumn hair before their eyes.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	寶命全形... :	
帝曰：人生有形，不離陰陽，天地合氣，別為九野，分為四時，月有小大，日有短長，萬物並至，不可勝量，虛實呿吟，敢問其方。歧伯曰：木得金而伐，火得水而滅，土得木而達，金得火而缺，水得土而絕，萬物盡然，不可勝竭。故鍼有懸布天下者五，黔首共餘食，莫知之也。一曰治神，二曰知養身，三曰知毒藥為真，四曰制砭石小大，五曰知府藏血氣之診。五法俱立，各有所先。今末世之刺也，虛者實之，滿者泄之，此皆眾工所共知也。若夫法天則地，隨應而動，和之者若響，隨之者若影，道無鬼神，獨來獨往。
		The Emperor said: Human life has form and cannot be separated from yin and yang. When the qi of Heaven and earth combine, they differentiate into nine regions; they divide into four seasons. The months have short or long durations, and days vary in length. All things arrive together, beyond measure. Deficiency and excess, subtle sounds and breaths—dare I ask for your method? Qibo said: Wood is cut by Metal; Fire is extinguished by Water; Earth is cultivated by Wood; Metal is damaged by Fire; and Water is blocked by Earth. All things follow this pattern, and such relationships are endless and cannot be fully exhausted. Therefore, there are five acupuncture methods that hang over the world like a banner; the common people benefit from them as if sharing food, yet they remain unaware of it. The first is regulating the spirit, the second is understanding how to nourish the body, the third is knowing which medicinal substances are genuine, the fourth is determining the size of moxa stones and needles, and the fifth is understanding the diagnosis of blood and qi in the zang-fu organs. When these five methods are all established, each has its own priority. In later times, acupuncture practice involves tonifying the deficient and purging the excessive—these are principles known to all medical practitioners. However, those who follow the laws of Heaven and Earth, responding in accordance with movement—harmony arises like an echo, and following occurs as a shadow. The Dao requires no ghosts or spirits; it proceeds alone, independent and self-directed.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	寶命全形... :	
帝曰：願聞其道。歧伯曰：凡刺之真，必先治神，五藏已定，九候已備，後乃存鍼，眾脈不見，眾凶弗聞，外肉相得，無以形先，可玩往來，乃施於人。人有虛實，五虛勿近，五實勿遠，至其當發，間不容𣊇。手動若務，鍼耀而勻，靜意視義，觀適之變，是謂冥冥，莫知其形，見其烏烏，見其稷稷，從見其飛，不知其誰，伏如橫弩，起如發機。帝曰：何如而虛，何如而實。歧伯曰：刺虛者須其實，刺實者須其虛，經氣已至，慎守勿失，深淺在志，遠近若一，如臨深淵，手如握虎，神無營於眾物。
		The Emperor said: I wish to hear about this Dao. Qibo said: The true practice of acupuncture must first regulate the spirit. Once the five zang organs are stabilized and the nine pulses are fully prepared, one may then hold the needle in readiness. When all other pulse patterns disappear and signs of misfortune become imperceptible, the external flesh harmonizes naturally. Do not act based on physical appearances alone; instead, observe the ebb and flow with care before applying acupuncture to a person. A person has conditions of deficiency or excess. When there are five signs of deficiency, do not approach; when there are five signs of excess, do not move away. At the moment treatment is appropriate, the timing must be precise—there is no room for hesitation. The hand moves with purpose, the needle shines evenly. With a calm mind and focused attention, observe the subtle changes in appropriateness. This is called "mingming"—a state of profound subtlety where one cannot discern its form. One sees only darkness or faint movement, as if watching birds take flight without knowing who sent them. It lies hidden like a crossbow drawn horizontally, then rises suddenly like a trigger released. The Emperor asked: What constitutes deficiency, and what constitutes excess? Qibo said: When treating deficiency, one must await its transformation into excess; when treating excess, one must wait for it to become deficient. Once the meridian qi has arrived, guard it carefully and do not let it escape. The depth of insertion depends on intention; whether near or far, treat them as one. Be as cautious as standing at the edge of a deep abyss, your hands firm as if gripping a tiger. Let your spirit remain undistracted by external things. 

26. 八正神明論 Discourse on the Eight Cardinal Directions and Divine Clarity

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	八正神明... :	
黃帝問曰：用鍼之服，必有法則焉，今何法何則。歧伯對曰：法天則地，合以天光。帝曰：願卒聞之。歧伯曰：凡刺之法，必候日月星辰四時八正之氣，氣定，乃刺之。是故天溫日明，則人血淖液，而衛氣浮，故血易寫，氣易行；天寒日陰，則人血凝泣，而衛氣沈。月始生，則血氣始精，衛氣始行；月郭滿，則血氣實，肌肉堅；月郭空，則肌肉減，經絡虛，衛氣去，形獨居。是以因天時而調血氣也。是以天寒無刺，天溫無疑。月生無寫，月滿無補，月郭空無治，是謂得時而調之。因天之序，盛虛之時，移光定位，正立而待之。故日月生而寫，是謂藏虛；月滿而補，血氣揚溢，絡有留血，命曰重實；月郭空而治，是謂亂經。陰陽相錯，真邪不別，沈以留止，外虛內亂，淫邪乃起。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "In using acupuncture needles, there must be certain rules and principles; what are these rules and principles now?" Qibo replied: "Follow the heavens and conform to the earth, harmonizing with celestial light." The Emperor said: "I wish to hear it in full." Qibo said: "In general, the method of acupuncture must await the qi of the sun, moon, stars, four seasons, and eight cardinal directions. Only when the qi is stable should one perform acupuncture." Therefore, on warm and sunny days, human blood becomes thin and fluid, while the defensive qi rises to the surface; thus, blood is easily drained and qi flows more readily. On cold and overcast days, human blood becomes stagnant and viscous, while the defensive qi sinks inward. When the moon begins to wax, blood and qi begin to become refined, and the defensive qi starts to circulate; when the lunar orbit is full, blood and qi are abundant, and muscles become firm; when the lunar orbit wanes, muscles weaken, meridians and collaterals become deficient, defensive qi departs, and the body remains alone. Therefore, one should regulate blood and qi according to celestial conditions. Thus, on cold days do not perform acupuncture; on warm days there is no hesitation. When the moon waxes, do not drain; when it is full, do not tonify; when the lunar orbit wanes, do no treatment. This is called regulating according to the right time. Following the order of heaven and the times of abundance or deficiency, shift light to determine position, stand upright and await accordingly. Therefore, draining when the sun and moon wax is called storing in deficiency; tonifying when the moon is full causes blood and qi to surge, leaving stagnant blood in the collaterals—this is called excessive excess. Performing treatment when the lunar orbit wanes is called disorder of the meridians. When yin and yang become disordered, true qi and pathogenic factors cannot be distinguished; stagnation occurs internally, the exterior becomes deficient while the interior is chaotic, and pathological influences arise.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	八正神明... :	
帝曰：星辰八正何候。歧伯曰；星辰者，所以制日月之行也。八正者，所以候八風之虛邪，以時至者也。四時者，所以分春秋冬夏之氣所在，以時調之也，八正之虛邪，而避之勿犯也。以身之虛，而逢天之虛，兩虛相感，其氣至骨，入則傷五藏，工候救之，弗能傷也，故曰：天忌不可不知也。
		The Emperor asked: "What are we to observe regarding the stars and the eight cardinal directions?" Qibo said: The stars are used to regulate the movements of the sun and moon. The eight cardinal directions are used to observe the pathogenic influences carried by the eight winds, which arrive according to their respective seasons. The four seasons are used to distinguish where the qi of spring, autumn, winter, and summer resides, so that one may regulate accordingly. The pathogenic influences associated with the eight cardinal directions should be avoided and not encountered. When the body is in a state of deficiency and encounters a time when heaven itself is deficient, these two deficiencies interact; their qi reaches deep into the bones. If it enters internally, it harms the five zang organs. However, if a skilled practitioner observes and intervenes promptly, no harm will be done. Therefore it is said: "One must not be unaware of heavenly taboos."

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	八正神明... :	
帝曰：善。其法星辰者，余聞之矣，願聞法往古者。歧伯曰；法往古者，先知鍼經也。驗於來今者，先知日之寒溫，月之虛盛，以候氣之浮沈，而調之於身，觀其立有驗也。觀其冥冥者，言形氣榮衛之不形於外，而工獨知之，以日之寒溫，月之虛盛，四時氣之浮沈，參伍相合而調之，工常先見之，然而不形於外，故曰觀於冥冥焉。通於無窮者，可以傳於後世也，是故工之所以異也，然而不形見於外，故俱不能見也。視之無形，嘗之無味，故謂冥冥，若神髣𨱰。虛邪者，八正之虛邪氣也。正邪者，身形若用力，汗出，腠理開，逢虛風，其中人也微，故莫知其情，莫見其形。上工救其萌牙，必先見三部九候之氣，盡調不敗而救之，故曰上工。下工救其已成，救其已敗。救其已成者，言不知三部九候之相失，因病而敗之也，知其所在者，知診三部九候之病脈處而治之。故曰守其門戶焉，莫知其情而見邪形也。
		The Emperor said: "Well said." I have heard about the method concerning stars, but I would like to hear about the methods based on ancient times. Qibo said: The method of following the past is first to understand the acupuncture classics. To test it in present times, one must first know the coldness or warmth of the day and the deficiency or abundance of the moon, to observe whether qi is superficial or deep, and regulate accordingly within the body. Observing this will yield verifiable results. Observing the unseen means understanding the form, qi, and functions of nutritive and defensive qi that are not evident externally. Only a skilled practitioner knows this by considering the coldness or warmth of days, the deficiency or abundance of moons, and the rising or sinking of seasonal qi, combining these factors to regulate accordingly. A skilled practitioner can foresee such conditions in advance; however, they do not manifest externally. Therefore it is said: "Observing what lies unseen." Those who understand the infinite can pass their knowledge to future generations. This is why skilled practitioners are different; however, since these conditions do not manifest externally, they cannot be perceived by ordinary people. They appear formless when observed and tasteless when tasted; thus they are called "unseen," like the faint presence of a deity. Pathogenic influences refer to the harmful qi associated with the eight cardinal directions. Righteous pathogenic factors occur when the body, as if exerting force, sweats and the pores open. If at this time one encounters a wind that takes advantage of deficiency, its invasion is subtle; thus no one knows its nature or sees its form. A superior practitioner intervenes at the initial stage, necessarily observing the qi of the three parts and nine pulses first. If all are regulated without failure, then intervention is made—thus such a person is called a superior practitioner. An inferior practitioner treats only after the condition has fully developed or already failed. Treating after the condition has already formed means not knowing how the three parts and nine pulses have become discordant, leading to failure in treatment. One who knows where the problem lies understands how to diagnose the pathological pulse patterns at the three parts and nine pulse positions and then treats accordingly. Therefore it is said: "Guarding the gates and households," for no one knows its true nature until pathogenic influences manifest visibly.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	八正神明... :	
帝曰：余聞補寫，未得其意。歧伯曰：寫必用方，方者，以氣方盛也，以月方滿也，以日方溫也，以身方定也，以息方吸而內鍼，乃復候其方吸而轉鍼，乃復候其方呼而徐引鍼，故曰寫必用方，其氣而行焉。補必用員，員者，行也，行者，移也，刺必中其榮，復以吸排鍼也。故員與方，非鍼也。故養神者，必知形之肥瘦，榮衛血氣之盛衰。血氣者，人之神，不可不謹養。
		The Emperor said: "I have heard of tonification and drainage, but I do not yet understand their meaning." Qibo said: "Drainage must follow the right timing. The 'right timing' means when qi is at its peak, when the moon is full, when the day is warm, and when the body has stabilized. Insert the needle during inhalation; then wait for another inhalation to rotate the needle, and finally slowly withdraw it during exhalation. Therefore, drainage must follow the right timing, allowing the qi to flow accordingly." Tonification must employ a circular method. "Circular" means movement; "movement" means shifting. When needling, one must strike the location of nutritive qi and then withdraw the needle while inhaling. Therefore, circular and square methods are not about the needles themselves. Thus, those who nurture spirit must know whether the body is fat or thin, and understand the abundance or decline of nutritive, defensive, blood, and qi. Blood and qi are the essence of a person; they must be carefully nurtured.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	八正神明... :	
帝曰：妙乎哉論也。合人形於陰陽四時，虛實之應，冥冥之期，其非夫子孰能通之。然夫子數言形與神，何謂形，何謂神，願卒聞之。歧伯曰：請言形，形乎形，目冥冥，問其所病，索之於經，慧然在前，按之不得，不知其情，故曰形。帝曰：何謂神。歧伯曰：請言神，神乎神，耳不聞，目明心開，而志先，慧然獨悟，口弗能言，俱視獨見，適若昏，昭然獨明，若風吹雲，故曰神。三部九候為之原，九鍼之論，不必存也。
		The Emperor said: "What an exquisite discussion this is." To harmonize the human form with yin and yang, the four seasons, responses of deficiency and excess, and to understand the unseen timing—without you, Master, who could possibly comprehend these? Yet you have often mentioned form and spirit. What is meant by "form," and what is meant by "spirit"? I wish to hear the full explanation. Qibo said: "Please allow me to explain form. When examining 'form,' the eyes see nothing clearly; when inquiring about the illness and searching for it along the meridians, although insight appears before one's mind, pressing on the body reveals no definite location, and its nature remains unknown—this is what is meant by 'form.'" The Emperor asked: "What is meant by spirit?" Qibo said: "Please allow me to explain spirit. Spirit is truly mysterious; the ears do not hear, yet the eyes are clear and the mind opens, with intention leading first. Insight arises independently, words cannot fully express it, all see together but only one perceives clearly. It appears as if in darkness, yet suddenly becomes bright, like wind scattering clouds—this is what is meant by spirit." The three parts and nine pulses serve as the foundation; discussions of the nine types of needles are not essential. 

27. 離合真邪 Separating and Combining True and Pathogenic Factors

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	離合真邪:	
黃帝問曰：余聞九鍼九篇，夫子乃因而九之，九九八十一篇，余盡通其意矣。經言氣之盛衰，左右傾移，以上調下，以左調右，有餘不足，補寫於榮輸，余知之矣。此皆榮衛之傾移，虛實之所生，非邪氣從外入於經也。余願聞邪氣之在經也，其病人何如，取之奈何。歧伯對曰：夫聖人之起度數，必應於天地，故天有宿度，地有經水，人有經脈。天地溫和，則經水安靜；天寒地凍，則經水凝泣；天暑地熱，則經水沸溢；卒風暴起，則經水波涌而隴起。夫邪之入於脈也，寒則血凝泣，暑則氣淖澤，虛邪因而入客，亦如經水之得風也，經之動脈，其至也亦時隴起，其行於脈中循循然，其至寸口中手也，時大時小，大則邪至，小則平，其行無常處，在陰與陽，不可為度，從而察之，三部九候，卒然逢之，早遏其路。吸則內鍼，無令氣忤，靜以久留，無令邪布，吸則轉鍼，以得氣為故，候呼引鍼，呼盡乃去，大氣皆出，故命曰寫。帝曰：不足者補之，柰何。歧伯曰：必先捫而循之，切而散之，推而按之，彈而怒之，抓而下之，通而取之，外引其門，以閉其神，呼盡內鍼，靜以久留，以氣至為故，如待所貴，不知日暮，其氣以至，適而自護，候吸引鍼，氣不得出，各在其處，推闔其門，令神氣存，大氣留止，故命曰補。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I have heard of the Nine Needles in nine chapters, and you, Master, added another nine to them, making a total of eighty-one chapters (nine nines), and I now fully understand their meaning. The classics say that the waxing and waning of vital energy causes a shift to the left or right, so one regulates the lower by means of the upper, and the right by means of the left; where there is excess or deficiency, one should tonify or drain at the collateral channels. I understand this already. These are all shifts in the nutritive and defensive qi, giving rise to deficiency or excess; they do not result from pathogenic factors entering the meridians from outside. I would like to hear about how a person is affected when pathogenic qi lodges in the meridians, and what methods should be used for treatment. Qibo replied: When a sage establishes measurements and calculations, they must correspond to heaven and earth. Therefore, the heavens have constellations with fixed positions, the earth has meridional rivers, and human beings have meridians and collaterals. When heaven and earth are warm and harmonious, the meridional waters remain tranquil; when the heavens are cold and the earth is frozen, the meridional water becomes stagnant and congealed; when the heavens are hot and the earth is warm, the meridional waters boil and overflow; when sudden winds suddenly arise, the meridional water surges in waves and rises in ridges. When pathogenic factors enter the vessels, cold causes blood to congeal and stagnate, while heat makes qi moist and slippery. Exogenous pathogens take advantage of these conditions to invade; this is similar to how meridional water is affected by wind. The movement of the arteries in the channels also rises suddenly at times. When they travel within the vessels, their flow is gradual. When reaching the cun (inch) portion of the radial artery on the wrist, their pulse varies in size—when large, it indicates the arrival of pathogenic factors; when small, it signifies normalcy. The movement has no fixed location and alternates between yin and yang aspects, making it impossible to measure by standard means. By carefully observing this, one examines the three sections and nine pulses (san bu jiu hou). If a sudden abnormality is encountered, one must block its path early on. Insert the needle during inhalation, so as not to disturb the qi. Keep it still for a prolonged time without allowing pathogenic factors to spread. Turn the needle during exhalation in order to obtain the arrival of qi (deqi). Wait until the patient inhales again before withdrawing the needle, and remove it completely at the end of the exhalation. When all the excess qi has been expelled, this method is called "draining" (sha). The Emperor asked: How should one tonify in cases of deficiency? Qibo said: One must first palpate and trace the area, press gently to disperse stagnation, push and apply pressure, tap to stimulate a response, grasp and guide it downward, then penetrate and extract it. Open the exterior gate externally while closing its spiritual aspect internally. Insert the needle during exhalation, keep it still for an extended time, waiting until the arrival of qi (deqi) is achieved. Treat it as one would await someone highly esteemed, regardless of how late it becomes. Once the qi has arrived and settled naturally in place, wait for the patient to inhale before withdrawing the needle so that the qi cannot escape but remains at its location. Close the gate by pushing inward, allowing the spirit-qi to remain and the great qi to stay put. This method is therefore called "tonifying" (bu).

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	離合真邪:	
帝曰：候氣奈何。歧伯曰；夫邪去絡入於經也，舍於血脈之中，其寒溫未相得，如涌波之起也，時來時去，故不常在。故曰：方其來也，必按而止之，止而取之，無逢其衝而寫之。真氣者，經氣也，經氣太虛，故曰：其來不可逢，此之謂也。故曰：候邪不審，大氣已過，寫之則真氣脫，脫則不復，邪氣復至，而病益蓄，故曰：其往不可追，此之謂也。不可挂以髮者，待邪之至時而發鍼寫矣，若先若後者，血氣已盡，其病不可下，故曰：知其可取如發機，不知其取如扣椎，故曰：知機道者不可挂以髮，不知機者扣之不發，此之謂也。帝曰：補寫奈何。歧伯曰：此攻邪也，疾出以去盛血，而復其真氣，此邪新客，溶溶未有定處也，推之則前，引之則止，逆而刺之，溫血也。刺出其血，其病立已。
		The Emperor asked: How should one await the arrival of qi? Qibo said: When pathogenic factors leave the collateral vessels and enter the meridians, they reside within the blood and vessels. At this stage, their cold or warm nature has not yet fully manifested; like surging waves, they come and go at times, hence they do not remain constant. Therefore it is said: When the pathogenic qi is about to arrive, one must press and halt it, then extract it. One should not meet its full force head-on in order to drain it. The true qi refers to the channel qi. When the channel qi is excessively deficient, this is why it is said: "Its arrival cannot be met," and this is what that statement means. Therefore it is said: If one fails to accurately await the pathogenic qi, and if the great qi has already passed, draining it would cause the true qi to escape. Once lost, the true qi cannot return, while the pathogenic factors will reappear, causing the illness to accumulate further. Hence it is said: "Its departure cannot be pursued," and this is what that means. It is said that one must not hesitate like a hair's breadth, but rather strike the needle to drain when the pathogenic qi arrives. If treatment occurs too early or too late, the blood and qi will have already dissipated, making the illness impossible to resolve. Hence it is said: "Knowing the right moment to act is as swift as releasing a trigger; not knowing the right time is like striking an empty hammer." This means that one who understands the principle of timing (ji dao) need not hesitate even for a hair's breadth, while one who does not understand this principle will strike in vain without effect. The Emperor asked: How should tonification and draining be performed? Qibo said: This is a method for expelling pathogenic factors. Withdraw the needle quickly to remove excessive blood and restore true qi. At this stage, the pathogen has just arrived and is still fluid without a fixed location; pushing it will cause it to advance, pulling back will halt its movement, and needling against its direction will warm the blood. If one punctures to release the blood, the illness is immediately resolved.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	離合真邪:	
帝曰：善。然真邪以合，波隴不起，候之柰何。歧伯曰：審捫循三部九候之盛虛而調之，察其左右上下相失及相減者，審其病藏以期之。不知三部者，陰陽不別，天地不分。地以候地，天以候天，人以候人，調之中府，以定三部，故曰：刺不知三部九候病脈之處，雖有大過且至，工不能禁也。誅罰無過，命曰：大惑，反亂大經，真不可復，用實為虛，以邪為真，用鍼無義，反為氣賊，奪人正氣，以從為逆，榮衛散亂，真氣已失，邪獨內著，絕人長命，予人天殃，不知三部九候，故不能久長。因不知合之四時五行，因加相勝，釋邪攻正，絕人長命。邪之新客來也，未有定處，推之則前，引之則止，逢而寫之，其病立已。
		The Emperor said: Well said. Yet when true and pathogenic qi have already combined, the pulse does not rise in waves; how then should one await it? Qibo said: Carefully palpate and examine the three sections and nine pulses to determine their excess or deficiency, then regulate accordingly. Observe whether there is disharmony between the left and right sides, upper and lower parts; carefully identify which internal organ is affected in order to anticipate its condition. Those who do not understand the three sections cannot distinguish yin from yang, nor can they differentiate heaven from earth. The earth section is used to observe the earth aspect, the heaven section for the heavenly aspect, and the human section for the human aspect. By regulating through the Zhongfu point (LU1), one can determine the three sections. Hence it is said: "If a physician does not know the three sections and nine pulses when diagnosing disease patterns, even if a serious condition is about to arise, he will be unable to prevent it." To punish without just cause is called great confusion. To act in violation of the fundamental principles disrupts the major meridians; true qi can no longer be restored. Treating excess as deficiency, and pathogenic factors as genuine conditions, using acupuncture without principle becomes a thief of vital energy, robbing people of their righteous qi. This turns harmony into disharmony, causing nutritive and defensive qi to become disordered and true qi to be lost. Pathogenic factors then remain within the body alone, cutting short one's life and bringing heavenly calamity upon them. Those who do not understand the three sections and nine pulses cannot achieve long-term success in their practice. Because they fail to integrate knowledge of the four seasons and five elements, they mistakenly apply counteracting methods, releasing pathogenic factors while attacking the righteous qi, thereby cutting short a person's life. When newly arrived pathogenic factors come in, they have not yet settled in a fixed location. Pushing them will cause them to advance; pulling back will stop their movement. If one meets and drains them at the right moment, the illness is immediately cured. 

28. 通評虛實論 Tongping Xushish Lun (Discourse on Assessing Deficiency and Excess)

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	通評虛實... :	
黃帝問曰：何謂虛實。歧伯對曰：邪氣盛則實，精氣奪則虛。帝曰：虛實何如。歧伯曰：氣虛者肺虛也，氣逆者足寒也，非其時則生，當其時則死。餘藏皆如此。帝曰：何謂重實。歧伯曰：所謂重實者，言大熱病，氣熱脈滿，是謂重實。帝曰：經絡俱實何如，何以治之。歧伯曰：經絡皆實，是寸脈急而尺緩也，皆當治之，故曰：滑則從，濇則逆也。夫虛實者，皆從其物類始，故五藏骨肉滑利，可以長久也。帝曰：絡氣不足，經氣有餘，何如。歧伯曰：絡氣不足，經氣有餘者，脈口熱而尺寒也，秋冬為逆，春夏為從，治主病者。帝曰：經虛絡滿何如。歧伯曰：經虛絡滿者，尺熱滿脈口寒濇也，此春夏死秋冬生也。帝曰：治此者奈何。歧伯曰：絡滿經虛，灸陰刺陽；經滿絡虛，刺陰灸陽。帝曰：何謂重虛。歧伯曰：脈氣上虛尺虛，是謂重虛。帝曰：何以治之。歧伯曰：所謂氣虛者，言無常也。尺虛者，行步恇然。脈虛者，不象陰也。如此者，滑則生，濇則死也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: What is meant by deficiency and excess? Qibo replied: When pathogenic qi is excessive, it results in excess; when the vital essence and qi are depleted, it results in deficiency. The Emperor asked: How do deficiency and excess manifest? Qibo said: Deficiency of qi indicates deficiency in the lungs; reversal of qi causes coldness in the feet. If this occurs at an inappropriate time, life is preserved; if it occurs at the appropriate time, death results. The other zang organs are all like this. The Emperor asked: What is meant by double excess? Qibo said: What is referred to as double excess means a severe febrile disease, where the qi and pulse are both hot and full; this is called double excess. The Emperor asked: What if both the meridians and collaterals are in a state of excess? How should it be treated? Qibo said: When both the meridians and collateral vessels are in a state of excess, it is indicated by an urgent pulse at the cun position and a slow pulse at the chi position. Both conditions should be treated accordingly; therefore, it is said that a slippery (smooth) pulse indicates harmony with treatment, while a 涩 (dry, rough) pulse indicates resistance to treatment. Deficiency and excess both originate from the nature of their respective substances; therefore, if the five zang organs' bones and flesh are smooth and supple, longevity can be achieved. The Emperor asked: What is the condition when the collateral qi is insufficient but the meridian qi is excessive? Qibo said: When the collateral qi is insufficient and the meridian qi is excessive, the pulse at the mouth (radial artery) feels hot while the chi position feels cold. This condition is adverse in autumn and winter but harmonious in spring and summer; treatment should focus on addressing the primary disease. The Emperor asked: What if the meridians are deficient while the collateral vessels are full? Qibo said: When the meridians are empty and the collaterals are full, it is manifested by a hot and full pulse at the chi position while the radial artery feels cold and 涩. In this case, death occurs in spring and summer, but life is preserved in autumn and winter. The Emperor asked: How should one treat such a condition? Qibo said: When the collaterals are full and the meridians are empty, moxibustion should be applied to yin points while acupuncture is used on yang points; when the meridians are full and the collaterals are empty, acupuncture should be performed on yin points while moxibustion is applied to yang points. The Emperor asked: What is meant by double deficiency? Qibo said: When both the pulse qi at the cun position and the chi position are deficient, it is called double deficiency. The Emperor asked: How should this be treated? Qibo said: What is referred to as qi deficiency means the condition lacks consistency. When the chi position is deficient, one walks with a timid and unstable gait. A pulse that is deficient does not resemble yin. In such cases, a slippery (smooth) condition indicates life, while a 涩 condition indicates death.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	通評虛實... :	
帝曰：寒氣暴上，脈滿而實何如。歧伯曰：實而滑則生，實而逆則死。帝曰：脈實滿，手足寒，頭熱，何如。歧伯曰：春秋則生，冬夏則死。脈浮而濇，濇而身有熱者死。帝曰：其形盡滿何如。歧伯曰：其形盡滿者，脈急大堅，尺濇而不應也，如是者，故從則生，逆則死。帝曰：何謂從則生，逆則死。歧伯曰：所謂從者，手足溫也。所謂逆者，手足寒也。帝曰：乳子而病熱，脈懸小者何如。歧伯曰：手足溫則生，寒則死。帝曰：乳子中風熱，喘鳴肩息者，脈何如。歧伯曰：喘鳴肩息者，脈實大也，緩則生，急則死。帝曰：腸澼便血何如。歧伯曰：身熱則死，寒則生。帝曰：腸澼下白沫何如。歧伯曰：脈沈則生，脈浮則死。帝曰：腸澼下膿血何如。歧伯曰：脈懸絕則死，滑大則生。帝曰：腸澼之屬，身不熱，脈不懸絕何如。歧伯曰：滑大者曰生，懸濇者曰死，以藏期之。帝曰：癲疾何如。歧伯曰：脈搏大滑，久自已；脈小堅急，死不治。帝曰：癲疾之脈，虛實何如。歧伯曰：虛則可治，實則死。帝曰：消癉虛實何如。歧伯曰：脈實大，病久可治；脈懸小堅，病久不可治。
		The Emperor asked: What if cold qi suddenly rises and the pulse is full and excessive? Qibo said: If it is excessive yet slippery, life will be preserved; but if it is excessive and against the normal flow, death will occur. The Emperor asked: What if the pulse is full and abundant, yet the hands and feet are cold while the head is hot? Qibo said: In spring and autumn, life will be preserved, but in winter and summer, death will occur. If the pulse is floating and 涩, and there is heat in the body, it indicates death. The Emperor asked: What if the entire physical form appears completely full? Qibo said: When the entire physical form is completely full, the pulse becomes taut, large, and firm, while the chi position shows 涩 and does not correspond; in such cases, harmony with treatment leads to life, but resistance leads to death. The Emperor asked: What is meant by "harmony with treatment leads to life" and "resistance leads to death"? Qibo said: What is referred to as harmony means the hands and feet are warm. What is referred to as resistance means the hands and feet are cold. The Emperor asked: What if a nursing mother suffers from fever with a suspended, small pulse? Qibo said: If the hands and feet are warm, life will be preserved; if they are cold, death will occur. The Emperor asked: In a nursing mother who has contracted wind-heat and experiences wheezing with labored breathing, what will the pulse be like? Qibo said: In cases of wheezing and labored breathing with shoulder lifting, the pulse is full and large; if it is slow, life will be preserved, but if it is taut, death will occur. The Emperor asked: What about dysentery with bloody stools? Qibo said: If there is body heat, death will occur; if there is coldness, life will be preserved. The Emperor asked: What about dysentery with white frothy discharge? Qibo said: If the pulse is deep, life will be preserved; if the pulse is superficial, death will occur. The Emperor asked: What about dysentery with pus and blood discharge? Qibo said: If the pulse is suspended or interrupted, death will occur; if it is slippery and large, life will be preserved. The Emperor asked: In cases of dysentery or similar conditions, what if the body is not hot and the pulse is not suspended or interrupted? Qibo said: A slippery and large pulse indicates life; a suspended and 涩 pulse indicates death, depending on the condition of the zang organs. The Emperor asked: What about epilepsy? Qibo said: If the pulse is forceful, large, and slippery, the condition will resolve on its own after some time; if the pulse is small, firm, and taut, it indicates an incurable death. The Emperor asked: In epilepsy, how do deficiency and excess manifest in the pulse? Qibo said: If it is deficient, it can be treated; if it is excessive, death will occur. The Emperor asked: In diabetes or wasting-thirst syndrome, how do deficiency and excess present? Qibo said: If the pulse is full and large, even if the disease has persisted for a long time, it can still be treated; if the pulse is suspended, small, and firm, and the disease has lasted for a long time, it cannot be cured.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	通評虛實... :	
帝曰：形度骨度脈度筋度，何以知其度也。帝曰：春亟治經絡，夏亟治經俞，秋亟治六府，冬則閉塞。閉塞者，用藥而少鍼石也。所謂少鍼石者，非癰疽之謂也，癰疽不得頃時回。癰不知所，按之不應手，乍來乍已，刺手太陰傍三痏與纓脈各二。掖癰大熱，刺足少陽五，刺而熱不止，刺手心主三，刺手太陰經絡者大骨之會各三。暴癰筋緛，隨分而痛，魄汗不盡，胞氣不足，治在經俞。腹暴滿，按之不下，取手太陽經絡者，胃之募也，少陰俞去脊椎三寸傍五，用員利鍼。霍亂，刺俞傍五，足陽明及上傍三。刺癇驚脈五，鍼手太陰各五，刺經太陽五，刺手少陰經絡傍者一，足陽明一，上踝五寸刺三鍼。
		The Emperor asked: How can one determine the standard measurements of body form, bone structure, pulse condition, and tendon strength? The Emperor said: In spring, the meridians and collaterals should be treated urgently; in summer, the meridian shu points should be addressed urgently; in autumn, the six fu organs should be attended to urgently; but in winter, they remain closed and sealed. During this period of closure and sealing, treatment should rely more on medicine and less on acupuncture or moxibustion. What is meant by "less use of acupuncture and stones" does not refer to abscesses or carbuncles, which cannot wait for a moment. For an abscess whose location is unknown, which does not respond to palpation and appears and disappears suddenly, acupuncture should be applied at three points beside the Taiyin meridian on the hand and two points along the ying vessels. For an axillary abscess with intense heat, acupuncture five points on the foot Shaoyang meridian. If the heat persists after needling, then needle three points on the Hand Jueyin (Heart) meridian and three points each at the major bone junctions of the Hand Taiyin meridians and their collateral vessels. Sudden abscesses with tense tendons, pain spreading along the meridians, incomplete perspiration, and insufficient qi of the bladder should be treated at the shu points of the affected meridians. In cases of sudden abdominal distension where pressing does not relieve it, select the collateral points of the Hand Taiyang meridian, which correspond to the mu point of the stomach. Additionally, needle five points on either side of the Shao Yin shu points located three cun lateral from the spine, using a yuanyi needle (a type of fine and sharp acupuncture needle). For cholera, needle five points beside the shu points, as well as three points on either side along the Foot Yangming meridian. For epilepsy and convulsions, needle five points along the affected meridians; acupuncture five points on each of the Hand Taiyin channels. Needle five points on the Bladder (Taiyang) meridian, one point on the collateral vessel beside the Hand Shaoyin channel, one point on the Foot Yangming channel, and three needles at a location five cun above the ankle.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	通評虛實... :	
凡治消癉仆擊，偏枯痿厥，氣滿發逆，肥貴人，則高梁之疾也。隔塞閉絕，上下不通，則暴憂之病也。暴厥而聾，偏塞閉不通，內氣暴薄也。不從內外中風之病，故瘦留著也。蹠跛，寒風濕之病也。黃帝曰：黃疸暴痛，癲疾厥狂，久逆之所生也。五藏不平，六府閉塞之所生也。頭痛耳鳴，九竅不利，腸胃之所生也。
		Generally, conditions such as diabetes or thirst syndrome, fainting and sudden collapse, hemiplegia, atrophy, cold extremities, qi congestion with upward reversal, and other similar diseases in wealthy and noble individuals are considered disorders caused by excessive consumption of rich foods. Obstruction and blockage, with no communication between upper and lower parts, indicate diseases arising from sudden or intense worry. Sudden syncope accompanied by deafness, partial obstruction and blockage without communication, indicates an abrupt internal qi surge. This is not a disease caused by external wind or internal injury; therefore, the condition lingers and becomes entrenched. Foot lameness is a disease caused by cold, wind, and dampness. The Yellow Emperor said: Jaundice with sudden pain, epilepsy, syncope, and mania are conditions that arise from prolonged disharmony. They result from imbalance in the five zang organs and obstruction of the six fu organs. Headache, tinnitus, and dysfunction of the nine orifices are conditions arising from disorders in the intestines and stomach. 

29. 太陰陽明論 Treatise on Taiyin and Yangming

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	太陰陽明... :	
黃帝問曰：太陰陽明為表裏，脾胃脈也，生病而異者何也。歧伯對曰：陰陽異位，更虛更實，更逆更從，或從內，或從外，所從不同，故病異名也。帝曰：願聞其異狀也。歧伯曰：陽者，天氣也，主外；陰者，地氣也，主內。故陽道實，陰道虛。故犯賊風虛邪者，陽受之；食飲不節起居不時者，陰受之。陽受之，則入六府，陰受之，則入五藏。入六府，則身熱不時臥，上為喘呼；入五藏則䐜滿閉塞，下為飱泄，久為腸澼。故喉主天氣，咽主地氣。故陽受風氣，陰受濕氣。故陰氣從足上行至頭，而下行循臂至指端；陽氣從手上行至頭，而下行至足。故曰：陽病者，上行極而下，陰病者，下行極而上。故傷於風者，上先受之；傷於濕者，下先受之。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: Taiyin and Yangming are exterior-interior pairs, the meridians of the spleen and stomach. Why do they produce different diseases? Qibo replied: Yin and Yang occupy different positions; they alternate between deficiency and excess, reversal and conformity. Sometimes the condition follows an internal pattern, sometimes an external one. Since their courses differ, the diseases have different names. The Emperor said: I wish to hear about their differing manifestations. Qibo said: Yang is the celestial qi, and it governs the exterior; Yin is the terrestrial qi, and it governs the interior. Therefore, the Yang path tends toward excess, while the Yin path tends toward deficiency. Thus, when one is affected by harmful winds or deficient pathogens, Yang is what receives them; Those who indulge in improper diet and irregular living habits suffer the effects on Yin. When Yang is affected, the pathogen enters the six fu organs; when Yin is affected, it enters the five zang organs. If the pathogen enters the six Fu organs, the person experiences fever and restlessness, with symptoms above manifesting as wheezing and labored breathing; If it enters the five Zang organs, there will be distension and obstruction in the upper abdomen, with symptoms below manifesting as diarrhea; if prolonged, this may develop into dysentery. Therefore, the throat governs celestial qi, while the pharynx governs terrestrial qi. Thus, Yang is affected by wind pathogen, and Yin is affected by dampness pathogen. Therefore, Yin qi rises from the feet to the head, then descends along the arms to the fingertips; Yang qi ascends from the hands to the head and then descends to the feet. Hence it is said: In diseases of Yang, the condition ascends to its extreme and then descends; in diseases of Yin, the condition descends to its extreme and then ascends. Therefore, when one is injured by wind pathogen, the upper part of the body is affected first; Therefore, when one is harmed by dampness, the lower part of the body is afflicted first.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	太陰陽明... :	
帝曰：脾病而四支不用，何也。歧伯曰：四支皆稟氣於胃，而不得至經，必因於脾，乃得稟也。今脾病不能為胃行其津液，四支不得稟水穀氣，氣日以衰，脈道不利，筋骨肌肉，皆無氣以生，故不用焉。
		The Emperor asked: When the spleen is diseased, why do the four limbs become paralyzed? Qibo said: The four limbs all receive qi from the stomach, but this qi cannot reach the meridians without the spleen; it must depend on the spleen to be received. Now, when the spleen is diseased and cannot transport body fluids for the stomach, the four limbs do not receive the qi of water and grains. As this qi gradually declines, the vessels become obstructed, and the tendons, bones, muscles all lack vital energy to sustain them; thus, they become paralyzed.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	太陰陽明... :	
帝曰：脾不主時，何也。歧伯曰：脾者土也，治中央，常以四時長四藏，各十八日寄治，不得獨主於時也。脾藏者，常著胃土之精也，土者，生萬物而法天地，故上下至頭足，不得主時也。
		The Emperor asked: Why is it said that the spleen does not preside over a specific season? Qibo said: The spleen belongs to Earth, and governs the central region. It constantly oversees the four seasons and supports the four Zang organs for a period of eighteen days each season, thus it cannot preside exclusively over any one season. The spleen, as an organ, constantly stores the essence of Earth from the stomach. Earth generates all things and follows the principles of Heaven and Earth; therefore, it extends from head to foot without presiding over a specific time or season.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	太陰陽明... :	
帝曰：脾與胃以膜相連耳，而能為之行其津液，何也。歧伯曰：足太陰者三陰也，其脈貫胃屬脾絡嗌，故太陰為之行氣於三陰。陽明者，表也，五藏六府之海也，亦為之行氣於三陽。藏府各因其經而受氣於陽明，故為胃行其津液。四支不得稟水穀氣，日以益衰，陰道不利，筋骨肌肉無氣以生，故不用焉。
		The Emperor asked: The spleen and the stomach are connected only by a membrane, yet the spleen can transport body fluids for them. Why is this so? Qibo said: The Foot Taiyin meridian is the third yin, and its vessel passes through the stomach, belongs to the spleen, and connects with the pharynx; therefore, the Taiyin transports qi among the three Yin meridians. Yangming is exterior in nature and serves as the sea of the five Zang and six Fu organs; it also transports qi among the three Yang meridians. The Zang and Fu organs each receive their respective meridian's qi from Yangming, hence the spleen transports body fluids for the stomach. The four limbs cannot receive the qi of water and grain, which gradually weakens. The Yin pathways become obstructed; tendons, bones, and muscles lack vital energy to be nourished, so they become paralyzed. 

30. 陽明脈解 Yangming Meridian Explanation

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	陽明脈解:	
黃帝問曰：足陽明之脈，病，惡人與火，聞木音則惕然而驚，鐘鼓不為動，聞木音而驚，何也，願聞其故。歧伯對曰：陽明者胃脈也，胃者，土也，故聞木音而驚者，土惡木也。帝曰：善。其惡火，何也。歧伯曰：陽明主肉，其脈血氣盛，邪客之則熱，熱甚則惡火。帝曰：其惡人何也。歧伯曰：陽明厥則喘而惋，惋則惡人。帝曰：或喘而死者，或喘而生者，何也。歧伯曰：厥逆連藏則死，連經則生。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "When the foot Yangming meridian is afflicted with disease, it causes aversion to people and fire. The patient becomes startled upon hearing the sound of wood, yet remains unaffected by the sounds of bells or drums. Why does he become startled specifically at the sound of wood? I would like to hear your explanation." Qibo replied: "The Yangming is the meridian of the stomach. The stomach belongs to Earth, so when a person becomes startled upon hearing the sound associated with Wood, it is because Earth is antagonistic to Wood." The Emperor said: "Well said." Why does he have an aversion to fire? Qibo said: "The Yangming governs the muscles, and its meridian has abundant blood and qi. When pathogenic influences invade it, they generate heat; when the heat becomes excessive, aversion to fire occurs." The Emperor asked: "Why does he have an aversion toward people?" Qibo said: "When the Yangming becomes rebellious, it causes shortness of breath and distress; when there is distress, aversion toward people occurs." The Emperor asked: "Some patients who have shortness of breath die, while others survive. Why is this so?" Qibo said: "If the reversal affects the internal organs, death occurs; if it only involves the meridians, survival is possible."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	陽明脈解:	
帝曰：善。病甚則棄衣而走，登高而歌，或至不食數日，踰垣上屋，所上之處，皆非其素所能也，病反能者何也。歧伯曰：四支者，諸陽之本也，陽盛則四支實，實則能登高也。帝曰：其棄衣而走者，何也。歧伯曰：熱盛於身，故棄衣欲走也。帝曰：其妄言罵詈不避親踈而歌者，何也。歧伯曰：陽盛則使人妄言罵詈不避親踈，而不欲食，不欲食，故妄走也。
		The Emperor said: "Well said." "When the disease is severe, the patient discards his clothes and runs about, climbs to high places and sings, or may even go several days without eating. He jumps over walls and ascends rooftops; these are all things he could not normally do. Why does illness cause such abilities?" Qibo said: "The four limbs are the foundation of all Yang. When Yang is excessive, the four limbs become full and strong; when they are full and strong, one can climb to high places." The Emperor asked: "Why does the patient discard his clothes and run about?" Qibo said: "Because heat is excessive in the body, he discards his clothes and wants to run." The Emperor asked: "Why does the patient speak nonsense, curse without regard for relatives or strangers, and sing?" Qibo said: "Excessive Yang causes a person to speak nonsense and curse without distinguishing between relatives or strangers, while also losing the desire to eat. Because he does not want to eat, he runs about aimlessly." 

31. 熱論 On Febrile Diseases

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	熱論:	
黃帝問曰：今夫熱病者，皆傷寒之類也，或愈或死，其死皆以六七日之閒，其愈皆以十日以上者，何也。不知其解，願聞其故。歧伯對曰：巨陽者，諸陽之屬也，其脈連於風府，故為諸陽主氣也。人之傷於寒也，則為病熱，熱雖甚不死，其兩感於寒而病者，必不免於死。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "Now, those who suffer from febrile diseases are all of the category of shanghan (injury by cold). Some recover and some die. Those who die do so within six or seven days; those who recover take more than ten days. Why is this?" I do not know the explanation, I would like to hear the reason. Qibo replied: "Juyang belongs to all yang meridians, its vessels connect to Fengfu, so it is the principal qi of all yang." When a person is injured by cold, it results in a febrile disease; although the heat may be severe, death does not occur. However, those who are affected by cold on both yin and yang sides and fall ill will inevitably die.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	熱論:	
帝曰：願聞其狀。歧伯曰：傷寒一日，巨陽受之，故頭項痛腰脊強。二日陽明受之，陽明主肉，其脈俠鼻絡於目，故身熱目疼而鼻乾，不得臥也。三日，少陽受之，少陽主膽，其脈循脇絡於耳，故胸脇痛而耳聾。三陽經絡皆受其病，而未入於藏者，故可汗而已。四日，太陰受之，太陰脈布胃中絡於嗌，故腹滿而嗌乾。五日，少陰受之，少陰脈貫腎絡於肺，繫舌本，故口燥舌乾而渴。六日，厥陰受之，厥陰脈循陰器而絡於肝，故煩滿而囊縮。三陰三陽，五藏六府，皆受病，榮衛不行，五藏不通，則死矣。
		The Emperor said: "I wish to hear the symptoms." Qibo said: "On the first day of shanghan, Juyang is affected; therefore, there is headache, neck pain, and stiffness in the waist and spine." On the second day, Yangming is affected.
