Yangming governs muscles; its vessels run alongside the nose and connect to the eyes, so there is body heat, eye pain, dryness of the nose, and inability to lie down. On the third day, Shaoyang is affected. Shaoyang governs the gallbladder; its vessels follow the sides of the chest and connect to the ears, so there is chest and flank pain and deafness in the ears. The three yang meridians are all affected by the disease, but it has not yet entered the zang (solid organs), so it can be resolved through perspiration alone. On the fourth day, Taiyin is affected. The Taiyin vessels spread through the stomach and connect to the pharynx; therefore, there is abdominal fullness and dryness of the throat. On the fifth day, Shaoyin is affected. The Shaoyin vessels pass through the kidneys and connect to the lungs, linking to the base of the tongue; therefore, there is dryness in the mouth, dry tongue, and thirst. On the sixth day, Jueyin is affected; the Jueyin vessels follow the genital organs and connect to the liver, so there is irritability, fullness, and retraction of the scrotum. When both the three yin and three yang meridians, as well as the five zang and six fu organs, are affected by disease, the nutritive and defensive qi cannot circulate, the five zang organs become blocked, and death occurs.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	熱論:	
其不兩感於寒者，七日巨陽病衰，頭痛少愈；八日，陽明病衰，身熱少愈；九日，少陽病衰，耳聾微聞；十日，太陰病衰，腹減如故，則思飲食；十一日，少陰病衰，渴止不滿，舌乾已而嚏；十二日，厥陰病衰，囊縱少腹微下，大氣皆去，病日已矣。帝曰：治之柰何。歧伯曰：治之各通其藏脈，病日衰已矣。其未滿三日者，可汗而已；其滿三日者，可泄而已。
		Those who are not affected by cold on both sides will see Juyang's condition wane on the seventh day, with headache slightly improved; On the eighth day, Yangming's condition wanes, and body heat is somewhat relieved; On the ninth day, Shaoyang's condition wanes, with deafness slightly improving so that sounds can be faintly heard; On the tenth day, Taiyin's condition wanes, abdominal distension decreases to normal, and there is a desire for food and drink; On the eleventh day, Shaoyin's condition wanes; thirst ceases, fullness is gone, dryness of the tongue subsides, and sneezing occurs; On the twelfth day, Jueyin's condition wanes: the scrotum relaxes and the lower abdomen slightly descends; all the pathogenic qi departs, and the disease gradually recedes. The Emperor asked: "How should this be treated?" Qibo said: "Treatment involves clearing the respective zang meridians; as each day passes, the disease will gradually subside." Those who have not yet reached three days may be treated with perspiration alone; Those whose condition has lasted for three full days may be treated by purgation alone.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	熱論:	
帝曰：熱病已愈，時有所遺者，何也。歧伯曰：諸遺者，熱甚而強食之，故有所遺也。若此者，皆病已衰，而熱有所藏，因其穀氣相薄，兩熱相合，故有所遺也。帝曰：善。治遺柰何。歧伯曰：視其虛實，調其逆從，可使必已矣。帝曰：病熱當何禁之。歧伯曰：病熱少愈，食肉則復，多食則遺，此其禁也。
		The Emperor asked: "After a febrile disease has been cured, sometimes there are lingering symptoms; why is this?" Qibo said: "Those who have residual symptoms did so because they overate while the heat was still severe, hence some effects remain." In such cases, although the disease has waned, heat remains stored within. When this combines with the warmth of food qi and the two heats interact, residual symptoms occur. The Emperor said: "Well said." How should the lingering symptoms be treated? Qibo said: "Examine whether it is deficiency or excess, regulate its disharmony and harmony; this can ensure the symptoms are definitely resolved." The Emperor asked: "What should be avoided in a febrile illness?" Qibo said: "After the fever has slightly improved, eating meat will cause relapse; overeating will leave residual symptoms. This is what should be avoided."

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	熱論:	
帝曰：其病兩感於寒者，其脈應與其病形何如。歧伯曰：兩感於寒者，病一日，則巨陽與少陰俱病，則頭痛口乾而煩滿；二日，則陽明與太陰俱病，則腹滿身熱，不欲食譫言；三日，則少陽與厥陰俱病，則耳聾囊縮而厥，水漿不入，不知人，六日死。帝曰：五藏已傷，六府不通，榮衛不行，如是之後，三日乃死，何也。歧伯曰：陽明者，十二經脈之長也，其血氣盛，故不知人，三日其氣乃盡，故死矣。凡病傷寒而成溫者，先夏至日者為病溫，後夏至日者為病暑，暑當與汗皆出，勿止。
		The Emperor asked: "In those who are affected by cold from both sides, how do the pulse and physical symptoms appear?" Qibo said: "Those affected by cold on both sides, on the first day, have Juyang and Shaoyin simultaneously affected; symptoms include headache, dry mouth, and irritability with fullness;" On the second day, Yangming and Taiyin are both affected; symptoms include abdominal distension, body heat, lack of appetite, and delirium; On the third day, Shaoyan and Jueyin are both affected; there is deafness, retracted scrotum, and cold extremities (厥), inability to take fluids, loss of consciousness, and death occurs on the sixth day. The Emperor asked: "When the five zang are already injured, the six fu are not communicating, and nutritive and defensive qi do not circulate—after such a condition, death occurs after three days. Why is this?" Qibo said: "Yangming is the longest of the twelve meridians, and its blood and qi are abundant; hence, loss of consciousness occurs. After three days, its qi is exhausted, so death follows." In general, diseases that originate from shanghan and develop into warm febrile conditions: those occurring before the summer solstice are called "wen" (warm), while those occurring after the summer solstice are termed "shu" (summer-heat). Summer-heat should be expelled along with perspiration; do not stop it. 

32. 刺熱 Treating Heat Illness

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺熱:	
肝熱病者，小便先黃，腹痛多臥身熱，熱爭，則狂言及驚，脇滿痛，手足躁，不得安臥，庚辛甚，甲乙大汗，氣逆則庚辛死，刺足厥陰少陽，其逆則頭痛員員，脈引衝頭也。心熱病者，先不樂，數日乃熱，熱爭則卒心痛，煩悶善嘔，頭痛面赤無汗，壬癸甚，丙丁大汗，氣逆則壬癸死，刺手少陰太陽。脾熱病者，先頭重頰痛，煩心顏青，欲嘔身熱，熱爭則腰痛不可用俛仰，腹滿泄，兩頷痛，甲乙甚，戊己大汗，氣逆則甲乙死，刺足太陰陽明。肺熱病者，先淅然厥，起毫毛惡風寒，舌上黃身熱。熱爭，則喘欬，痛走胸膺背，不得大息，頭痛不堪，汗出而寒，丙丁甚，庚辛大汗，氣逆則丙丁死，刺手太陰陽明，出血如大豆，立已。腎熱病者，先腰痛䯒痠，苦渴數飲，身熱，熱爭，則項痛而強，䯒寒且痠，足下熱，不欲言，其逆，則項痛員員澹澹然，戊己甚，壬癸大汗，氣逆則戊己死，刺足少陰太陽，諸汗者，至其所勝日，汗出也。肝熱病者，左頰先赤，心熱病者，顏先赤，脾熱病者，鼻先赤，肺熱病者，右頰先赤，腎熱病者，頤先赤，病雖未發，見赤色者刺之，名曰治未病。熱病從部所起者，至期而已；其刺之反者，三周而已；重逆則死。諸當汗者，至其所勝日，汗大出也。諸治熱病，以飲之寒水，乃刺之，必寒衣之，居止寒處，身寒而止也。
		Those suffering from liver-heat illness first experience yellow urine, abdominal pain, frequent lying down due to body heat. When the heat intensifies, they may speak wildly and be startled, suffer fullness and pain in the flanks, have restless hands and feet, and be unable to lie down comfortably. The condition worsens on Gengxin days, improves with profuse sweating on Jiayi days. If qi becomes reversed, death occurs on Gengxin days. Acupuncture should target the Foot-Jueyin and Foot-Shaoyang meridians. When the condition is reversed, it causes a dull headache, with pulse sensations drawing up to the head. Those suffering from heart-heat illness initially feel unhappy, and after several days develop heat. When the heat intensifies suddenly, they experience chest pain, restlessness, frequent vomiting, headache, red face, and no perspiration. The condition worsens on Ren Gui days, improves with profuse sweating during Bing Ding days. If qi becomes reversed, then death occurs on Ren Gui days. Acupuncture should be applied to the Hand-Shaoyin and Hand-Taiyang meridians. Those suffering from spleen-heat illness first experience heavy head, cheek pain, a troubled mind, bluish facial complexion, desire to vomit, and body heat. When the fever intensifies, it causes severe lower back pain that prevents bending forward or backward, abdominal fullness with diarrhea, and pain in both sides of the jaw. The condition worsens on Jia Yi days, improves with profuse perspiration during Wu Ji days. If qi becomes reversed then death occurs on Jia Yi days. Acupuncture should be administered to the Foot-Taiyin and Foot-Yangming meridians. Those suffering from lung-heat illness first feel a shivering sensation, with the body hairs standing on end and aversion to wind and cold, followed by yellow coating on the tongue and body heat. When the heat intensifies, it causes shortness of breath and coughing, with pain radiating to the chest, upper abdomen, and back, making deep breathing difficult. Headache becomes unbearable, accompanied by perspiration and chills. The condition worsens during Bing Ding days, improves with profuse sweat on Geng Xin days. If qi is reversed then death occurs during Bing Ding days. Acupuncture should be performed on the Hand-Taiyin and Hand-Yangming meridians; bleeding as much as a broad bean will bring immediate relief. Those suffering from kidney-heat illness first experience lower back pain, soreness in the legs, intense thirst with frequent drinking, and body heat. When this fever intensifies, it leads to neck pain and stiffness, cold and sour sensations in the thighs, warmth beneath the feet, and reluctance to speak. If the condition is reversed, then a dull and heavy headache occurs. The illness worsens on Wu Ji days, improves with profuse sweats during Ren Gui days. If qi becomes reversed death occurs on Wu Ji days. Acupuncture should be directed at the Foot-Shaoyin and Foot-Taiyang meridians. Those who perspire will experience sweating again on the day corresponding to their element's overcoming cycle. In those with liver-heat illness, the left cheek turns red first. In those with heart-heat illness, the facial complexion turns red first. In those suffering from spleen-heat disease, the nose turns red first. For lung-heat illness, it is the right cheek that reddens first. And in kidney-heat illness, the chin (yí) turns red first. Even before symptoms manifest, if a red color appears on these areas, acupuncture should be performed. This practice is known as "treating disease before it occurs." Heat illness that originates from its corresponding region will resolve by the appointed time. If acupuncture is applied incorrectly, it will take three cycles to recover; Severe reversal of treatment leads to death. Those who are supposed to perspire will experience profuse sweating on the day corresponding to the element that overcomes their condition. For treating all types of heat illness, one should drink cold water and receive acupuncture. The patient must be dressed in cool clothing and reside in a cool place until the body cools down and symptoms cease.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺熱:	
熱病先胸脇痛，手足躁，刺足少陽，補足太陰，病甚者，為五十九刺。熱病始手臂痛者，刺手陽明太陰而汗出止。熱病始於頭首者，刺項太陽而汗出止。熱病始於足脛者，刺足陽明而汗出止。熱病先身重骨痛，耳聾好暝，刺足少陰，病甚，為五十九刺。熱病先眩冒而熱，胸脇滿，刺足少陰少陽。太陽之脈，色榮顴骨，熱病也，榮未交，曰今且得汗，待時而已。與厥陰脈爭見者，死期不過三日。其熱病內連腎，少陽之脈色也。少陽之脈，色榮頰前，熱病也，榮未交，曰今且得汗，待時而已，與少陰脈爭見者，死期不過三日。
		In heat illness where chest and flank pain precede restlessness in the hands and feet, acupuncture should be applied to the Foot-Shaoyang meridian for dispersion and to the Foot-Taiyin meridian for tonification. In severe cases, fifty-nine needle insertions are performed. In heat illness that begins with arm and leg pain, acupuncture should be administered to the Hand-Yangming and Hand-Taiyin meridians until perspiration stops. In heat illness that originates in the head, acupuncture should be applied to points on the Bladder (Taiyang) meridian at the neck until perspiration ceases. In heat illness that originates in the feet and shins, acupuncture should be administered to points on the Foot-Yangming meridian until perspiration ceases. In heat illness where body heaviness, bone pain, deafness, and a tendency to drowsiness occur first, acupuncture should be applied at the Foot-Shaoyin meridian. In severe cases, it involves fifty-nine needle insertions. In heat illness where dizziness and a sensation of confusion precede the fever, with chest and flank fullness, acupuncture should be administered at points on the Foot-Shaoyin andfoot-Shaoyang meridians . The Taiyang pulse, when its color manifests in the prominence of the cheekbones, indicates a heat illness. If this radiance has not yet intermingled with other signs, it means that perspiration will occur soon; one need only wait for the appointed time. If the Taiyang pulse appears in conflict with the Jueyin pulse, death will not be delayed beyond three days. The heat illness that internally connects to the kidney is indicated by the color of the Shaoyang pulse. The Shaoyang pulse, when its color is manifested in the prominence before the cheeks, indicates a heat illness. When this radiance has not yet mingled with other signs, it signifies that perspiration will soon occur; one need only await the appointed time. If it appears in conflict with the Shaoyin pulse, then death will not be delayed by more than three days.

3
打開字典
	刺熱:	
熱病，氣穴三椎下間主胸中熱，四椎下間主鬲中熱，五椎下間主肝熱，六椎下間主脾熱，七椎下間主腎熱，榮在骶也。項上三椎陷者，中也。頰下逆顴為大瘕，下牙車為腹滿，顴後為脇痛，頰上者，鬲上也。
		In heat illness, the acupoints located between the vertebrae serve specific functions: three vertebrae below (the third thoracic vertebra) governs heat in the chest; four vertebrae below governs heat between the diaphragm and abdomen; five vertebrae below relates to liver heat; six vertebrae below corresponds to spleen heat; seven vertebrae below is associated with kidney heat. The brilliance of these conditions manifests at the sacrum. The depression located three vertebrae down from the back of the neck corresponds to the middle (i.e., the heart or central region). A prominence below the cheekbone indicates a large abdominal mass; swelling beneath the jaw signifies fullness in the abdomen. A prominence behind the cheekbone corresponds to flank pain, while the upper part of the cheeks relates to the area above the diaphragm. 

33. 評熱病論 Treatise on Febrile Disorders

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	評熱病論:	
黃帝問曰：有病溫者，汗出輒復熱，而脈躁疾不為汗衰，狂言不能食，病名為何。歧伯對曰：病名陰陽交，交者死也。帝曰：願聞其說。歧伯曰：人所以汗出者，皆生於穀，穀生於精，今邪氣交爭於骨肉而得汗者，是邪卻而精勝也，精勝，則當能食而不復熱。復熱者邪氣也，汗者精氣也，今汗出而輒復熱者，是邪勝也，不能食者，精無俾也，病而留者，其壽可立而傾也。且夫熱論曰：汗出而脈尚躁盛者死。今脈不與汗相應，此不勝其病也，其死明矣。狂言者是失志，失志者死。今見三死，不見一生，雖愈必死也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "There is a patient with febrile illness, who perspires but the fever recurs immediately. The pulse is rapid and forceful without diminishing after sweating, and he speaks incoherently and cannot eat. What is this disease called?" Qibo replied: "This illness is named 'Yinyang Jiao,' which means the yin and yang have become entangled. When they become entangled, it indicates a fatal condition." The Emperor said: "I would like to hear your explanation." Qibo said: "The reason people perspire is because it originates from the grains, and the grains originate from essence. Now, when pathogenic qi contends within the bones and flesh yet sweating occurs, this indicates that the pathogenic factors have been repelled while the essence has prevailed. When essence prevails, one should be able to eat and no longer experience fever." The recurrence of fever is due to pathogenic qi, while perspiration arises from the essence-qi. Now that sweating occurs yet fever recurs immediately, this indicates the dominance of pathogenic factors. The inability to eat means the essence has no support. If illness persists and lingers, life expectancy can be said to collapse instantly." Moreover, according to the Treatise on Febrile Diseases: "If perspiration occurs yet the pulse remains rapid and forceful, it is a fatal condition." Now that the pulse does not correspond with the sweating, this indicates an inability to overcome the illness. The outcome of death is clearly evident. Incoherent speech indicates a loss of mental clarity; when there is a loss of mental clarity, it signifies death. Now three signs of death are evident while no sign of life remains; even if the condition appears to improve, death is inevitable.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	評熱病論:	
帝曰：有病身熱汗出煩滿，煩滿不為汗解，此為何病。歧伯曰：汗出而身熱者，風也，汗出而煩滿不解者，厥也，病名曰風厥。帝曰：願卒聞之。歧伯曰：巨陽主氣，故先受邪，少陰與其為表裏也，得熱則上從之，從之則厥也。帝曰：治之柰何。歧伯曰：表裏刺之，飲之服湯。
		The Emperor asked: "There is a patient who has body fever, perspiration, and irritability with fullness. The irritability and fullness do not subside even after sweating. What illness is this?" Qibo said: "Perspiration with body fever indicates wind. Perspiration with unresolved irritability and fullness indicates reversal of qi, and the illness is called 'Fengjue.'" The Emperor said: "I would like to hear a complete explanation." Qibo said: "Taiyang governs the exterior qi, so it is affected first by pathogenic factors. Shaoyin forms an exterior-interior relationship with Taiyang; when heat arises, it ascends along this pathway, and following this leads to reversal of qi (jue)." The Emperor asked: "How should this be treated?" Qibo said: "Needle the exterior and interior meridians, and administer Fu Tang (a herbal decoction)."

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	評熱病論:	
帝曰：勞風為病何如。歧伯曰：勞風法在肺下，其為病也，使人強上冥視，唾出若涕，惡風而振寒，此為勞風之病。帝曰：治之柰何。歧伯曰：以救俛仰。巨陽引精者三日，中年者五日，不精者七日，欬出青黃涕，其狀如膿，大如彈丸，從口中若鼻中出，不出則傷肺，傷肺則死也。
		The Emperor asked: "What are the symptoms of illness caused by labor-induced wind?" Qibo said: "Labor-induced wind primarily affects the area beneath the lungs. The illness manifests as stiffness in the neck and upward gaze, expectoration resembling mucus, aversion to wind with chills, and this is termed 'labor-induced wind' disease." The Emperor asked: "How should it be treated?" Qibo said: "Treatment aims to relieve the stiffness in looking up and down." For Taiyang to draw out the pathogenic factors, three days are required for those in vigorous health, five days for middle-aged individuals, and seven days for those with weak constitution. Coughing will produce greenish-yellow mucus resembling pus, about the size of a wadcake; it should emerge either from the mouth or nose. If it does not exit, the lungs will be damaged, and damage to the lungs leads to death.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	評熱病論:	
帝曰：有病腎風者，面胕痝然壅，害於言，可刺不。歧伯曰：虛不當刺，不當刺而刺，後五日其氣必至。帝曰：其至何如。歧伯曰：至必少氣時熱，時熱從胸背上至頭，汗出，手熱，口乾苦渴，小便黃，目下腫，腹中鳴，身重難以行，月事不來，煩而不能食，不能正偃，正偃則欬，病名曰風水，論在刺法中。帝曰：願聞其說。歧伯曰：邪之所湊，其氣必虛，陰虛者，陽必湊之，故少氣時熱而汗出也。小便黃者，少腹中有熱也。不能正偃者，胃中不和也。正偃則欬甚，上迫肺也。諸有水氣者，微腫先見於目下也。帝曰：何以言。歧伯曰：水者陰也，目下亦陰也，腹者至陰之所居，故水在腹者，必使目下腫也。真氣上逆，故口苦舌乾，臥不得正偃，正偃則欬出清水也。諸水病者，故不得臥，臥則驚，驚則欬甚也。腹中鳴者，病本於胃也。薄脾則煩不能食，食不下者，胃脘隔也。身重難以行者，胃脈在足也。月事不來者，胞脈閉也，胞脈者屬心而絡於胞中，今氣上迫肺，心氣不得下通，故月事不來也。帝曰：善。
		The Emperor asked: "There is a patient with kidney wind, whose face swells and appears puffy, causing difficulty in speech. Can acupuncture be used to treat this?" Qibo said: "If the condition is deficient, needling should not be performed. If acupuncture is mistakenly applied when it should not be used, then five days later the qi will surely arrive." The Emperor asked: "What will happen when this qi arrives?" Qibo said: "When it arrives, the patient will experience shortness of breath and intermittent fever. The fever spreads from the chest and back up to the head, accompanied by perspiration, hot hands, dry mouth with bitter taste and thirst, yellow urine, swelling below the eyes, abdominal gurgling, body heaviness making movement difficult, absence of menstrual flow, irritability with inability to eat, and inability to lie on one's back; lying flat causes coughing. This illness is called 'fengshui,' and its treatment is discussed in the section on acupuncture methods." The Emperor said: "I would like to hear an explanation of this." Qibo said: "Where pathogenic factors gather, the qi must be deficient. When yin is deficient, yang will surely gather there; hence, shortness of breath, intermittent fever, and perspiration occur." Yellow urine indicates heat in the lower abdomen. lung The inability to lie flat on one's back indicates disharmony within the stomach. Lying flat causes severe coughing, as the condition presses upward upon the lungs. All conditions involving water retention manifest initially with slight swelling beneath the eyes. The Emperor asked: "On what basis is this said?" Qibo said: "Water belongs to yin, and the area beneath the eyes is also yin. The abdomen is where extreme yin resides; therefore, when water accumulates in the abdomen, it must cause swelling beneath the eyes." When true qi reverses upward, this causes bitter taste in the mouth and dry tongue; lying flat is impossible, and if one lies flat, clear fluid will be coughed up. All water-related illnesses prevent a person from lying down; when they do lie down, they become startled, and this startlement worsens the coughing. Abdominal gurgling indicates that the disease originates in the stomach. When the spleen is affected, irritability and inability to eat occur; if food cannot be swallowed, it indicates obstruction at the gastric orifice. Body heaviness making movement difficult occurs because the stomach meridian runs through the feet. Absence of menstrual flow indicates obstruction in the uterine vessels. The uterine vessels belong to the heart and connect with the uterus; now, qi has reversed upward into the lungs, preventing heart qi from descending, hence menstruation does not occur. The Emperor said: "Well said." 

34. 逆調論 Ni Diaolun

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	逆調論:	
黃帝問曰：人身非常溫也，非常熱也，為之熱而煩滿者何也。歧伯對曰：陰氣少而陽氣勝，故熱而煩滿也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: The human body is not usually warm, nor is it usually hot; why then does heat cause discomfort and fullness? Qibo replied: This occurs because yin qi is deficient while yang qi prevails, hence the heat and discomfort with fullness.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	逆調論:	
帝曰：人身非衣寒也，中非有寒氣也，寒從中生者何。歧伯曰：是人多痺氣也，陽氣少，陰氣多，故身寒如從水中出。
		The Emperor asked: The human body is not cold from lacking clothing, nor does it contain cold qi internally; what causes internal cold to arise? Qibo said: This person has excessive bi qi. Yang qi is deficient, and yin qi is excessive; therefore, the body feels cold as if emerging from water.

3
打開字典
	逆調論:	
帝曰：人有四支熱，逢逄風寒，如灸如火者，何也。歧伯曰：是人者，陰氣虛，陽氣盛，四支者陽也，兩陽相得而陰氣虛少，少水不能滅盛火，而陽獨治，獨治者不能生長也，獨勝而止耳，逢風而如灸如火者，是人當肉爍也。
		The Emperor asked: Some people have heat in their four limbs and encounter wind-cold, feeling as if burned by fire; what is the reason for this? Qibo said: This person has deficient yin qi and excessive yang qi. The four limbs belong to yang; when two yangs combine while yin qi is weak and insufficient, the small amount of water cannot extinguish the intense fire, so yang dominates alone. When yang governs alone, it cannot promote growth but merely prevails until a limit is reached. When encountering wind, they feel as if burned by fire or 灸; this indicates that their flesh will be scorched away.

4
打開字典
	逆調論:	
帝曰：人有身寒，湯火不能熱，厚衣不能溫，然不凍慄，是為何病。歧伯曰：是人者，素腎氣勝，以水為事，太陽氣衰，腎脂枯不長，一水不能勝兩火，腎者水也，而生於骨，腎不生，則髓不能滿，故寒甚至骨也。所以不能凍慄者，肝一陽也，心二陽也，腎孤藏也，一水不能勝二火，故不能凍慄，病名曰骨痺，是人當攣節也。
		The Emperor asked: Some people feel cold in their bodies, and neither hot water nor fire can warm them, nor can thick clothing provide warmth; yet they do not shiver from the cold. What kind of illness is this? Qibo said: This person has long had excessive kidney qi, and their life involves much water-related activity. The taiyang qi is weakened; the kidney fat dries up and does not grow. One measure of water cannot overcome two fires. The kidneys belong to water and are rooted in the bones. If the kidneys do not function properly, then marrow cannot be fully produced, hence coldness reaches even the bones. Therefore, they do not shiver from cold: the liver is one yang, and the heart is two yangs. The kidneys are a solitary zang organ; one measure of water cannot overcome the combined heat of the two fires, so they do not experience chills. This illness is called gu bi (bone arthralgia), and such people will eventually suffer from joint stiffness.

5
打開字典
	逆調論:	
帝曰：人之肉苛者，雖近衣絮，猶尚苛也，是謂何疾。歧伯曰：榮氣虛，衛氣實也，榮氣虛則不仁，衛氣虛則不用，榮衛俱虛，則不仁且不用，肉如故也，人身與志不相有，曰死。
		The Emperor asked: When a person's flesh feels rough, even when close to clothing or padding, the sensation of roughness remains; what is this illness called? Qibo said: This occurs when the ying qi is deficient and the wei qi is excessive. When ying qi is insufficient, there is numbness; when wei qi is insufficent, movement is impaired. If both ying and wei qi are deficient, then there will be both numbness and immobility, yet the flesh appears unchanged. When a person's body and spirit no longer interact with each other, it indicates death.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	逆調論:	
帝曰：人有逆氣不得臥而息有音者，有不得臥而息無音者，有起居如故而息有音者，有得臥行而喘者，有不得臥不能行而喘者，有不得臥臥而喘者，皆何藏使然，願聞其故。歧伯曰：不得臥而息有音者，是陽明之逆也，足三陽者下行，今逆而上行，故息有音也。陽明者，胃脈也，胃者六府之海，其氣亦下行，陽明逆不得從其道，故不得臥也。下經曰：胃不和則臥不安，此之謂也。夫起居如故而息有音者，此肺之絡脈逆也，絡脈不得隨經上下，故留經而不行，絡脈之病人也微，故起居如故而息有音也。夫不得臥臥則喘者，是水氣之客也，夫水者循津液而流也，腎者水藏，主津液，主臥與喘也。帝曰：善。
		The Emperor asked: Some people have reversed qi and cannot lie down, with audible breathing; others cannot lie down but breathe without sound. Some live normally as before yet have audible breathing. Others can lie down and walk but experience shortness of breath. Still others cannot lie down or walk and are short of breath. There are also those who cannot lie down and, when they do, still experience shortness of breath. What zang organs cause these conditions? I wish to hear the reason behind them. Qibo said: Those who cannot lie down and have audible breathing suffer from reversal of yangming qi. The three yin meridians of the foot normally descend, but now they move upward in reverse; hence, there is audible breathing. Yangming refers to the stomach meridian. The stomach is the sea of the six fu organs, and its qi also descends naturally. When yangming qi reverses and cannot follow its normal path, it causes an inability to lie down. The Lower Classic says: "When the stomach is disharmonized, sleep will be restless." This refers precisely to that condition. Those who live normally as before but have audible breathing suffer from reversed collateral vessels of the lung. The collaterals cannot follow the main meridians in their upward and downward movement, so they remain stagnant within the meridians without flowing properly. Since disorders of the collateral vessels are mild, daily activities appear normal while breathing remains audible. Those who cannot lie down and experience shortness of breath when they do suffer from the invasion of water qi. Water follows body fluids in its circulation, and the kidneys are the zang organ governing water; they control body fluids and also regulate lying down and breathing difficulties. The Emperor said: Well said. 

35. 瘧論 Malaria Theory

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	瘧論:	
黃帝問曰：夫痎瘧皆生於風，其蓄作有時者，何也。歧伯對曰：瘧之始發也，先起於毫毛，伸欠乃作，寒慄鼓頷，腰脊俱痛，寒去則內外皆熱，頭痛如破，渴欲冷飲。帝曰：何氣使然，願聞其道。歧伯曰：陰陽上下交爭，虛實更作，陰陽相移也。陽并於陰，則陰實而陽虛，陽明虛，則寒慄鼓頷也；巨陽虛，則腰背頭項痛；三陽俱虛，則陰氣勝，陰氣勝，則骨寒而痛；寒生於內，故中外皆寒；陽盛則外熱；陰虛則內熱，外內皆熱，則喘而渴；故欲冷飲也。此皆得之夏傷於暑，熱氣盛，藏於皮膚之內，腸胃之外，此榮氣之所舍也。此令人汗空踈，腠理開，因得秋氣，汗出遇風，及得之以浴，水氣舍於皮膚之內，與衛氣并居。衛氣者，晝日行於陽，夜行於陰，此氣得陽而外出，得陰而內薄，內外相薄，是以日作。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "Malaria is all caused by wind; yet its recurrence follows a fixed pattern. Why is this so?" Qibo replied: "When malaria first begins to manifest, it starts with the hair follicles. The person yawns and stretches, then chills set in, causing shivering and jaw clenching, along with pain in the waist and spine. When the cold passes, both inside and outside become hot; the headache is so severe that it feels as if the head will split open, and there is a strong thirst for cold drinks." The Emperor asked: "What kind of qi causes this? I wish to hear your explanation." Qibo said: "This is caused by the struggle between yin and yang, with upper and lower energies contending. The alternation of deficiency and excess occurs as yin and yang shift positions." When yang combines with yin, then yin becomes excessive while yang is deficient. When Yangming (the Stomach and Large Intestine meridians) is deficient, chills and jaw clenching occur. When Taiyang (the Bladder and Small Intestine meridians), the great yang, is deficient, pain occurs in the waist, back, head, and neck; When all three yang meridians are deficient, yin energy prevails. When yin energy prevails, the bones feel cold and painful; Cold arises internally, so both inside and outside feel cold; When yang is excessive, external heat occurs; When yin is deficient, internal heat arises. When both inside and outside are hot, the person experiences shortness of breath and thirst; Therefore, they desire cold drinks." These conditions all arise from injuries sustained in summer due to excessive heat. The intense heat energy is stored within the skin but outside the intestines and stomach; this is where the nutritive qi resides. This causes the pores to remain open and perspiration to flow freely, allowing the body's surface to become loose. As a result, when autumn qi is encountered, sweat emerging from the body meets wind; or alternatively, if one takes a bath at this time, water qi lodges within the skin and combines with defensive qi. Defensive qi travels through the yang meridians during the day and through the yin meridians at night. When this qi encounters yang, it moves outward; when it encounters yin, it becomes internally constrained. The conflict between internal and external forces is why symptoms occur daily.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	瘧論:	
帝曰：其間日而作者何也。歧伯曰：其氣之舍深，內薄於陰，陽氣獨發，陰邪內著，陰與陽爭不得出，是以間日而作也。帝曰：善。其作日晏與其日早者，何氣使然。歧伯曰：邪氣客於風府，循膂而下，衛氣一日一夜大會於風府，其明日日下一節，故其作也晏，此先客於脊背也，每至於風府則腠理開，腠理開則邪氣入，邪氣入則病作，以此日作稍益晏也。其出於風府，日下一節，二十五日下至骶骨，二十六日入於脊內，注於伏膂之脈，其氣上行，九日出於缺盆之中，其氣日高，故作日益早也。其間日發者，由邪氣內薄於五藏，橫連募原也，其道遠，其氣深，其行遲，不能與衛氣俱行，不得皆出，故間日乃作也。
		The Emperor asked: "Why do some cases manifest every other day?" Qibo said: "In such cases, the pathogenic qi lodges deeply and is internally constrained by yin. Yang energy alone attempts to expel it, but the yin pathogens adhere from within. The struggle between yin and yang cannot resolve itself immediately; therefore, symptoms occur every other day." The Emperor said: "Well said." What causes the symptoms to manifest later in the day or earlier? What kind of qi leads to this? Qibo said: "When pathogenic qi lodges in the Fengfu (GV16) region and follows along the spine downward, defensive qi completes a full cycle through the body once every day and night, converging at Fengfu. Therefore, on the following day, it reaches one segment lower. Hence, symptoms manifest later in the day. This occurs because the pathogenic factors initially lodge in the back of the spine. Each time the defensive qi arrives at Fengfu, the pores open; when the pores open, pathogenic qi enters, and when pathogenic qi enters, illness manifests. Therefore, with each passing day, the onset becomes increasingly delayed." When the pathogenic qi exits Fengfu and moves downward one segment each day, after twenty-five days it reaches the sacrum. On the twenty-sixth day, it enters within the spine and lodges in the Fu Lü meridian (the deep muscles along the back). From there, its energy ascends; on the ninth day, it emerges at the level of the Yuesong (ST12) region. As this qi rises higher each day, the onset of symptoms occurs increasingly earlier. Those cases that manifest every other day occur because the pathogenic qi has penetrated deeply into the five zang organs and spreads laterally to the Mu Yuan (a region between the skin and muscles). The pathway is long, the depth of invasion is great, and its movement is slow. It cannot keep pace with defensive qi and thus cannot be expelled all at once; therefore, symptoms appear every other day.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	瘧論:	
帝曰：夫子言衛氣每至於風府，腠理乃發，發則邪氣入，入則病作。今衛氣日下一節，其氣之發也不當風府，其日作者柰何。歧伯曰：此邪氣客於頭項循膂而下者也，故虛實不同，邪中異所，則不得當其風府也。故邪中於頭項者，氣至頭項而病；中於背者，氣至背而病；中於腰脊者，氣至腰脊而病；中於手足者，氣至手足而病。衛氣之所在，與邪氣相合，則病作。故風無常府，衛氣之所發，必開其腠理，邪氣之所合，則其府也。帝曰：善。
		The Emperor said: "You have stated that whenever defensive qi reaches Fengfu, the pores and skin open. When they open, pathogenic qi enters; when it enters, illness manifests." Now that defensive qi moves downward one segment each day and does not reach Fengfu on a given day, how can symptoms manifest daily? Qibo said: "This is because the pathogenic qi originally lodged in the head and neck region, then followed along the spine downward. Therefore, since the deficiency and excess vary, and the location of invasion differs, it does not necessarily correspond to Fengfu." Therefore, when pathogenic qi invades the head or neck region, illness occurs as defensive qi reaches that area; when it lodges in the back, illness occurs as the qi reaches the back; when it lodges in the waist or spine, illness occurs when the qi reaches there; and when it lodges in the hands and feet, illness occurs as the defensive qi arrives at those locations. When defensive qi reaches a location where pathogenic qi is present, the two meet, and symptoms manifest. Therefore, wind does not have a fixed location of lodging; wherever defensive qi flows and causes the pores to open, that is where pathogenic qi may combine with it. That site becomes its "residence." The Emperor said: "Well said."

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	瘧論:	
夫風之與瘧也，相似同類，而風獨常在，瘧得有時而休者何也。歧伯曰：風氣留其處，故常在；瘧氣隨經絡沈以內薄，故衛氣應乃作。帝曰：瘧先寒而後熱者，何也。歧伯曰：夏傷於大暑，其汗大出，腠理開發，因遇夏氣淒滄之水寒，藏於腠理皮膚之中，秋傷於風，則病成矣。夫寒者，陰氣也，風者，陽氣也，先傷於寒，而後傷於風，故先寒而後熱也，病以時作，名曰寒瘧。帝曰：先熱而後寒者，何也。歧伯曰：此先傷於風而後傷於寒，故先熱而後寒也，亦以時作，名曰溫瘧。其但熱而不寒者，陰氣先絕，陽氣獨發，則少氣煩冤，手足熱而欲嘔，名曰癉瘧。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "Wind and malaria are similar in nature. Yet wind illness persists constantly, while malaria has intervals of remission. Why is this so?" Qibo said: "Wind qi remains localized at its site of invasion, hence it persists constantly; malaria pathogenic qi follows the meridians and deeply invades inwardly, so symptoms manifest only when defensive qi responds to it." The Emperor asked: "In malaria, why does cold precede heat?" Qibo said: "In summer, injury occurs from intense heat, causing profuse sweating and opening of the pores. If at this time one encounters cold water or cool autumn qi, it lodges within the pores and skin. When further injured by wind in autumn, the disease manifests." Cold is yin qi, and wind is yang qi. When one first suffers injury from cold and later from wind, the symptoms begin with cold followed by heat. The illness manifests at specific times; this condition is called "huan miao" (cold malaria). The Emperor asked: "Why does some cases of the disease begin with heat followed by cold?" Qibo said: "This occurs when one is first injured by wind and later by cold, hence the symptoms begin with heat followed by cold. It also manifests at specific times; this disease is called 'wen miao' (warm malaria)." Those cases where there is only heat and no cold occur because yin qi has already been depleted, allowing yang energy to manifest alone. This results in shortness of breath, irritability, hot hands and feet, and a desire to vomit; this condition is called "gu miao" (hot malaria).

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	瘧論:	
帝曰：夫經言有餘者寫之，不足者補之。今熱為有餘，寒為不足。夫瘧者之寒，湯火不能溫也，及其熱，冰水不能寒也，此皆有餘不足之類。當此之時，良工不能止，必須其自衰，乃刺之，其故何也，願聞其說。歧伯曰；經言無刺熇熇之熱，無刺渾渾之脈，無刺漉漉之汗，故為其病逆未可治也。夫瘧之始發也，陽氣并於陰，當是之時，陽虛而陰盛，外無氣，故先寒慄也。陰氣逆極，則復出之陽，陽與陰復并於外，則陰虛而陽實，故先熱而渴。夫瘧氣者，并於陽則陽勝，并於陰則陰勝，陰勝則寒，陽勝則熱。瘧者，風寒之氣不常也，病極則復。至病之發也，如火之熱，如風雨不可當也。故經言曰：方其盛時必毀，因其衰也，事必大昌。此之謂也。夫瘧之未發也，陰未并陽，陽未并陰，因而調之，真氣得安，邪氣乃亡，故工不能治其已發，為其氣逆也。帝曰：善。攻之柰何，早晏何如。歧伯曰：瘧之且發也，陰陽之且移也，必從四末始也，陽已傷，陰從之，故先其時堅束其處，令邪氣不得入，陰氣不得出，審候見之在孫絡盛堅而血者皆取之，此真往而未得并者也。帝曰：瘧不發，其應何如。歧伯曰：瘧氣者，必更盛更虛，當氣之所在也，病在陽，則熱而脈躁；在陰，則寒而脈靜；極則陰陽俱衰，衛氣相離，故病得休；衛氣集，則復病也。
		The Emperor said: "As the classics state, for excess conditions one should drain, and for deficiency conditions one should tonify." Now heat is considered an excess condition, while cold is a deficiency. In malaria, the cold experienced during an attack cannot be alleviated by hot or warm substances; when heat occurs, it cannot be cooled even with ice water. These are all examples of excess and deficiency conditions. At such times, even a skilled physician cannot stop the symptoms; treatment must wait until the condition naturally subsides before acupuncture can be applied. What is the reason for this? I would like to hear your explanation. Qibo said: The classics state that one should not needle intense heat, confused and disordered pulses, or profuse sweating. This is because the condition is still in a disharmonious phase and cannot yet be treated. When malaria first manifests, yang energy combines with yin. At this time, yang is deficient and yin is excessive; there is no external qi to resist the pathogen, so cold shivering occurs first. When yin energy reaches its extreme of disharmony, yang returns outwardly. Yang and yin then combine externally, resulting in deficiency of yin and excess of yang; thus, heat and thirst occur first. The pathogenic qi of malaria combines with yang when yang prevails, and with yin when yin prevails. When yin prevails, cold occurs; when yang prevails, heat occurs. Malaria is caused by inconsistent wind-cold qi; the disease reaches an extreme and then reverses. When the illness manifests, it is as intense as fire's heat, as unstoppable as a storm. Therefore, the classics say: "When it is at its peak, resistance will only cause damage; when treatment follows its decline, success will surely be great." This is what is meant by that statement. When malaria has not yet manifested, yin and yang have not yet combined. If one regulates the body at this stage, true qi can remain stable and pathogenic qi will dissipate. Hence, a physician cannot treat it effectively once symptoms are fully manifest, because by then the qi is already in disharmony. The Emperor said: "Well said." How should one treat it? What is the best time—early or late? Qibo said: "Before malaria manifests, yin and yang are about to shift. The symptoms will first appear in the extremities. When yang is already injured, yin follows suit. Therefore, before the onset, one should firmly bind the affected area so that pathogenic qi cannot enter and true yin cannot escape. Carefully observe for signs; when visible on the fine collateral vessels as they become full, firm, and blood-filled, these points should be needled. This is the case of true qi moving but not yet combined with pathogenic factors." The Emperor asked: "If malaria does not manifest, how will it respond?" Qibo said: "The pathogenic qi of malaria must alternate between excess and deficiency. When the disease is located in yang, there will be heat and a rapid pulse; when it is located in yin, there will be cold and a calm pulse; When the condition reaches its extreme, both yin and yang become deficient, and defensive qi separates from them. Therefore, the illness enters a period of remission; when defensive qi gathers again, the disease recurs.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	瘧論:	
帝曰：時有間二日或至數日發，或渴或不渴，其故何也。歧伯曰：其間日者，邪氣與衛氣客於六府，而有時相失，不能相得，故休數日乃作也。瘧者，陰陽更勝也，或甚或不甚，故或渴或不渴。帝曰：論言夏傷於暑，秋必病瘧，今瘧不必應者何也。歧伯曰：此應四時者也。其病異形者，反四時也。其以秋病者寒甚，以冬病者寒不甚，以春病者惡風，以夏病者多汗。
		The Emperor asked: "Sometimes malaria occurs every two days or even after several days; sometimes there is thirst and sometimes not. What causes this?" Qibo said: "The cases where symptoms occur every other day are due to pathogenic qi and defensive qi lodging in the six fu organs, which at times lose contact with each other and cannot meet. Therefore, after several days of remission, the illness recurs." Malaria is a condition in which yin and yang alternately prevail. Sometimes one prevails strongly, sometimes not so much; therefore, there may be thirst or no thirst. The Emperor asked: "The classics say that injury from summer heat will inevitably lead to malaria in autumn. Yet not everyone who is injured in summer develops malaria. Why is this?" Qibo said: "This refers to those whose conditions correspond with the four seasons." Those cases where the disease presents differently are exceptions that do not follow the seasonal pattern. Those who fall ill in autumn experience severe cold; those who fall ill in winter experience less cold. Those who fall ill in spring suffer from aversion to wind, and those who fall ill in summer have excessive sweating.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	瘧論:	
帝曰：夫病溫瘧與寒瘧而皆安舍，舍於何藏。歧伯曰：溫瘧者，得之冬中於風，寒氣藏於骨髓之中，至春則陽氣大發，邪氣不能自出，因遇大暑，腦髓爍，肌肉消，腠理發泄，或有所用力，邪氣與汗皆出，此病藏於腎，其氣先從內出之於外也。如是者，陰虛而陽盛，陽盛則熱矣，衰則氣復反入，入則陽虛，陽虛則寒矣，故先熱而後寒，名曰溫瘧。帝曰：癉瘧何如。歧伯曰：癉瘧者，肺素有熱氣盛於身，厥逆上衝，中氣實而不外泄，因有所用力，腠理開，風寒舍於皮膚之內、分肉之閒而發，發則陽氣盛，陽氣盛而不衰，則病矣。其氣不及於陰，故但熱而不寒，氣內藏於心，而外舍於分肉之閒，令人消爍脫肉，故命曰癉瘧。帝曰：善。
		The Emperor asked: "In cases of warm malaria and cold malaria, where do the pathogenic factors reside? In which zang organ do they lodge?" Qibo said: "Warm malaria arises when one is injured by wind during winter, and cold pathogenic qi lodges deep within the bone marrow. By spring, yang energy becomes very active; however, the pathogen cannot expel itself. When summer heat arrives, the brain and marrow are scorched, muscles waste away, pores open up, or if physical exertion occurs, both pathogens and sweat emerge together. This disease resides in the kidney, with its qi first emerging from within to the exterior." In such cases, yin is deficient while yang is excessive. Excess yang causes heat; when it declines, the qi returns inwardly. When this happens, yang becomes deficient again, and deficiency of yang results in cold. Therefore, symptoms begin with heat followed by cold—this condition is called "wen miao" (warm malaria). The Emperor asked: "What about 'gu miao' (hot malaria)?" Qibo said: "Gu miao (hot malaria) occurs when the lungs have long been affected by internal heat, which accumulates in the body. Rebellious qi surges upward; middle jing is full but does not discharge outwardly. If at this time physical exertion causes the pores to open up, wind-cold lodges within the skin and between muscle layers, leading to onset of illness. When it manifests, yang energy is excessive; if yang remains excessive without decline, disease results." Since its energy does not reach yin, there is only heat without cold. The pathogenic qi resides internally in the heart and externally lodges between muscle layers, causing wasting of flesh and tissue loss; thus it is called "gu miao" (hot malaria). The Emperor said: "Well said." 

36. 刺瘧 Acupuncture for Malaria

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺瘧:	
足太陽之瘧，令人腰痛頭重，寒從背起，先寒後熱，熇熇暍暍然，熱止汗出，難已，刺郄中出血。足少陽之瘧，令人身體解㑊，寒不甚，熱不甚，惡見人，見人心惕惕然，熱多汗出甚，刺足少陽。足陽明之瘧，令人先寒洒淅洒淅寒甚久乃熱，熱去汗出喜見日月光火氣，乃快然，刺足陽明跗上，足太陰之瘧，令人不樂，好大息，不嗜食，多寒熱汗出，病至則善嘔，嘔已乃衰，即取之。足少陰之瘧，令人嘔吐甚，多寒熱，熱多寒少；欲閉戶牖而處，其病難已。足厥陰之瘧，令人腰痛少腹滿，小便不利，如癃狀，非癃也，數便，意恐懼氣不足，腹中悒悒，刺足厥陰。
		Malaria caused by the Bladder Meridian makes a person experience lower back pain and heavy head, with cold starting from the back. The symptoms begin with chills followed by fever, feeling hot and restless, and after the fever subsides, perspiration occurs but the condition is difficult to resolve; acupuncture at the 郗 points should be used to draw blood. Malaria caused by the Gallbladder Meridian makes a person feel physically exhausted and weak, with mild chills and mild fever. The patient dislikes seeing people, and when encountering others, feels anxious and uneasy. There is more heat with profuse sweating; acupuncture should be applied to the Foot Shaoyang meridian. Malaria caused by the Stomach Meridian makes a person first experience chills, feeling cold and shivering for a long time before fever sets in. After the fever subsides with sweating, the patient prefers to see sunlight, moonlight, or fire, which brings relief; acupuncture should be performed at points on the dorsum of the foot along the Foot Yangming meridian. Malaria caused by the Spleen Meridian makes a person unhappy and prone to deep sighing, with poor appetite. The condition is marked by frequent chills, fever, and sweating. When the illness occurs, the patient tends to vomit; after vomiting, symptoms subside; acupuncture should then be applied. Malaria caused by the Foot Shaoyin meridian makes a person vomit severely, with frequent chills and fever, where heat symptoms predominate over cold ones. The patient wishes to stay indoors with doors and windows closed; this condition is difficult to cure. Malaria caused by the Foot Jueyin meridian makes a person experience lower backache, fullness in the lower abdomen, and difficulty urinating, resembling symptoms of urinary retention, though it is not actual retention. The patient urinates frequently but feels fearful due to qi deficiency, with a sense of oppression and discomfort in the abdomen; acupuncture should be applied at points along the Foot Jueyin meridians.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺瘧:	
肺瘧者，令人心寒，寒甚熱，熱間善驚，如有所見者，刺手太陰陽明。心瘧者，令人煩心甚欲得清水反寒多，不甚熱，刺手少陰。肝瘧者，令人色蒼蒼然太息，其狀若死者，刺足厥陰見血。脾瘧者，令人寒，腹中痛，熱則腸中鳴，鳴已汗出，刺足太陰。腎瘧者，令人洒洒然，腰脊痛宛轉大便難，目眴眴然，手足寒，刺足太陽少陰。胃瘧者，令人且病也，善飢而不能食，食而支滿腹大，刺足陽明太陰橫脈出血。
		Pulmonary malaria causes a person to feel cold in the heart, with severe chills followed by fever. During the febrile episodes, the patient is easily startled and may experience illusions as if seeing something; acupuncture should be applied at the points of the Hand Taiyin and Yangming meridians. Cardiac malaria causes a person to experience great mental distress, with an intense desire for cold water and frequent chills, though the fever is not severe; acupuncture should be applied at points of the Hand Shaoyin meridian. Hepatic malaria causes a person's complexion to appear bluish and makes them sigh deeply, with symptoms resembling those of death; acupuncture should be applied at foot Jueyin points until blood appears. Spleen malaria causes a person to feel chilled and experience abdominal pain, with intestinal rumbling during fever; after the rumbling subsides, sweating occurs; acupuncture should be applied at Foot Taiyin points. Kidney malaria causes a person to shiver, experience lower back and spine pain with difficulty turning over or having bowel movements, have blurred vision, and feel cold in the hands and feet; acupuncture should be applied at both Foot Taiyang and Shaoyin points. Stomach malaria causes a person, when the illness occurs, to feel hungry yet unable to eat; even after eating, they experience fullness and bloating of the abdomen. Acupuncture should be applied at horizontal vessels on the Foot Yangming and Taiyin meridians until blood is drawn.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺瘧:	
瘧發身方熱，刺跗上動脈，開其空出其血，立寒，瘧方欲寒刺手陽明太陰足陽明太陰。
		When malaria attacks and the body is just beginning to feel hot, acupuncture should be applied at pulsating points on the dorsum of the feet. The skin should be pierced to release blood; this will immediately induce chills. When malaria is about to cause chills, acupuncture should be administered at the Hand Yangming, Taiyin, and Foot Yangming, Taiyin meridians.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺瘧:	
瘧脈滿大，急刺背俞，用中鍼傍伍胠俞各一，適肥瘦出其血也。瘧脈小實，急灸脛少陰，刺指井。瘧脈滿大，急刺背俞，用五胠俞背俞各一，適行至於血也。瘧脈緩大虛，便宜用藥，不宜用鍼，凡治瘧先發，如食頃乃可以治，過之則失時也，諸瘧而脈不見，刺十指閒出血，血去必已，先視身之赤如小豆者盡取之。
		When the pulse in malaria is full and large, acupuncture should be promptly applied at the back shu points. Use medium needles to insert one needle each beside the five wu points and xu shu points on both sides; adjust the amount of blood drawn according to the patient's body size. When the pulse in malaria is small but firm, immediate moxibustion should be applied on the shao yin meridian of the leg, and acupuncture at the well points of the fingers. When the pulse in malaria is large and full, acupuncture should be urgently applied at the back shu (BL) points. Insert one needle each into five xu shu and back shu points; adjust the treatment according to the condition until blood is drawn. When the pulse in malaria is slow, large, and weak, it is more appropriate to use medicine rather than acupuncture. In general, when treating malaria before its onset, treatment should be administered within a short time like that of eating; if delayed, the opportunity will be missed. For all types of malaria where the pulse cannot be clearly detected, bloodletting at the spaces between the ten fingers is recommended; once the blood is drawn, the condition will resolve. First observe red spots on the body as small as beans and remove them completely.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺瘧:	
瘧者，其發各不同時，察其病形，以知其何脈之病也。先其發時如食頃而刺之，一刺則衰，二刺則知，三刺則已，不已，刺舌下兩脈出血，不已，刺郄中盛經出血，又刺項已下俠脊者必已。舌下兩脈者，廉泉也。
		Malaria presents with different onset times; by observing its symptoms, one can determine which meridian is affected. If acupuncture is applied just before the onset of malaria, within a time equivalent to eating, one treatment will reduce the symptoms; two treatments will make it clear that improvement has occurred; three treatments should cure it. If not cured, bloodletting from the two vessels under the tongue should be performed. If still ineffective, draw blood from the 郗 points where meridians are full of energy. If this also fails, acupuncture at points below the nape along both sides of the spine will certainly resolve the condition. The two vessels under the tongue refer to Lianquan (CV23).

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺瘧:	
刺瘧者，必先問其病之所先發者，先刺之。先頭痛及重者，先刺頭上及兩額兩眉閒出血。先項背痛者，先刺之。先腰脊痛者，先刺郄中出血，先手臂痛者，先刺手少陰陽明十指閒。先足脛痠痛者，先刺足陽明十指閒出血。風瘧，瘧發則汗出惡風，刺三陽經背俞之血者，䯒痠痛甚按之不可名曰胕髓病，以鑱鍼鍼絕骨出血，立已。身體小痛，刺至陰。諸陰之井無出血，閒日一刺。瘧不渴，閒日而作，刺足太陽。渴而閒日作，刺足少陽。溫瘧汗不出，為五十九刺。
		When treating malaria with acupuncture, one must first ask which part of the body is affected first by the illness and treat that area first. If headache or heaviness occurs first, acupuncture with bloodletting should be applied first at points on the head and between both eyebrows and cheeks. If neck and back pain occur first, acupuncture should be administered there first. If lower back or spine pain occurs first, bloodletting at 郗 points should be performed first. If arm and hand pain occur first, acupuncture with bleeding should be applied to the Hand Shaoyin, Yangming meridians and between the ten fingers. If pain and soreness in the legs or shins occur first, bloodletting should be performed at the Foot Yangming points between the ten toes. Wind malaria causes sweating and aversion to wind during an attack. For this condition, blood should be drawn from the shu points of the three yang meridians on the back. If there is severe pain and soreness that cannot be precisely localized—described as a disease affecting the marrow-like tissues—a thin needle (can zhen) should be used to puncture the juegu point until blood appears, which will immediately relieve the condition. For mild body pain, acupuncture should be applied at Zhiyin (BL67). The well points of all yin meridians should not involve bloodletting; instead, they should be needled once every other day. For malaria without thirst and occurring intermittently on alternate days, acupuncture should be applied to the foot taiyang meridian. For malaria with thirst that occurs intermittently every other day, acupuncture should be administered to the Foot Shaoyang (GB) meridian. In cases of warm malaria where perspiration does not occur, fifty-nine acupuncture points should be treated. 

37. 氣厥論 Qi Jue Lun

1
打開字典
	氣厥論:	
黃帝問曰：五藏六府，寒熱相移者何。歧伯曰：腎移寒於肝，癰腫少氣。脾移寒於肝，癰腫筋攣。肝移寒於心，狂隔中。心移寒於肺，肺消，肺消者飲一溲二，死不治。肺移寒於腎，為涌水，涌水者，按腹不堅，水氣客於大腸，疾行則鳴濯濯如囊裹漿，水之病也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "Regarding the five zang and six fu organs, what are those in which cold and heat transfer to one another?" Qibo said: "When the kidney transmits cold to the liver, it results in abscesses and swelling with shortness of breath." When the spleen transmits cold to the liver, there will be abscesses, swelling, and cramping in the tendons. When the liver transmits cold to the heart, it causes mania and obstruction in the middle jiao. When the heart transmits cold to the lungs, the lungs are consumed; when the lungs are consumed, one drinks a lot but urinates twice as much, and this condition is fatal and incurable. When the lungs transmit cold to the kidneys, it results in yong shui. Yong shui is characterized by a soft abdomen when pressed, with water qi lodging in the large intestine; rapid walking causes gurgling sounds like water sloshing in a bag, which is a disease of water retention.

2
打開字典
	氣厥論:	
脾移熱於肝，則為驚衂。肝移熱於心，則死。心移熱於肺，傳為鬲消。肺移熱於腎，傳為柔痓。腎移熱於脾，傳為虛，腸澼死，不可治。
		When the spleen transmits heat to the liver, it leads to sudden fright and epistaxis (nosebleed). When the liver transmits heat to the heart, death occurs. When the heart transmits heat to the lungs, it is transmitted as ge xiao (a type of consumption or wasting disease). When the lungs transmit heat to the kidneys, it is transmitted as rou zhi (a condition characterized by spasm and rigidity). When the kidneys transmit heat to the spleen, it is transmitted as xu (deficiency), leading to intestinal discharge and death; this condition cannot be treated.

3
打開字典
	氣厥論:	
胞移熱於膀胱，則癃溺血。膀胱移熱於小腸，鬲腸不便，上為口麋。小腸移熱於大腸，為虙瘕為沈。大腸移熱於胃，善食而瘦入謂之食亦。胃移熱於膽，亦曰食亦。膽移熱於腦，則辛頞鼻淵，鼻淵者，濁涕下不止也，傳為衂衊瞑目，故得之氣厥也。
		When the pao transmits heat to the bladder, it results in difficulty urinating and hematuria (blood in urine). When the bladder transmits heat to the small intestine, there is obstruction of the intestines with difficulty in defecation, and upward it causes excessive salivation. When the small intestine transmits heat to the large intestine, it results in fu jia (a type of abdominal mass) and chen (a condition marked by heaviness or oppression). When the large intestine transmits heat to the stomach, it causes increased appetite but weight loss; this is called shi yi. When the stomach transmits heat to the gallbladder, it is also referred to as shi yi. When the gallbladder transmits heat to the brain, it causes a bitter taste in the nose and excessive nasal discharge. When there is nasal discharge, turbid mucus flows continuously; this may be transmitted as epistaxis (nose bleeding) and visual impairment, hence it is caused by qi 厥 (qi inversion). 

38. 欬論 Treatise on Cough

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	欬論:	
黃帝問曰：肺之令人欬，何也。歧伯對曰：五藏六府，皆令人欬，非獨肺也。帝曰：願聞其狀。歧伯曰：皮毛者，肺之合也，皮毛先受邪氣，邪氣以從其合也。其寒飲食入胃，從肺脈上至於肺，則肺寒，肺寒則外內合，邪因而客之，則為肺欬。五藏各以其時受病，非其時，各傳以與之。人與天地相參，故五藏各以治，時感於寒，則受病，微則為欬，甚者為泄為痛。乘秋則肺先受邪，乘春則肝先受之，乘夏則心先受之，乘至陰則脾先受之，乘冬則腎先受之。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "Why does the lung cause coughing?" Qibo replied: "The five zang and six fu organs can all cause coughing; it is not solely the lung." The Emperor said: "I would like to hear about their characteristics." Qibo said: "The skin and hair are the counterparts of the lung. The skin and hair first receive pathogenic factors, which then follow their counterpart to affect the lung." When cold food and drink enter the stomach, they follow the lung meridian upward to reach the lung; this causes cold in the lung. When the lung is cold, its exterior-interior counterparts become harmonized, allowing pathogenic factors to take residence there, resulting in cough originating from the lung. The five zang organs each receive illness according to their respective seasons; when it is not their season, they transmit the condition to one another. Human beings correspond with heaven and earth; therefore, the five zang organs are each governed by their respective seasons. When they are affected by cold during their governing season, illness occurs. A mild condition manifests as coughing, while a severe one results in diarrhea or pain. When the season is autumn, the lung first receives pathogenic factors; when it is spring, the liver first receives them; when it is summer, the heart first receives them; during the most yin time (late summer), the spleen first receives them; and when it is winter, the kidney first receives them.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	欬論:	
帝曰：何以異之。歧伯曰：肺欬之狀，欬而喘息有音，甚則唾血。心欬之狀，欬則心痛。喉中介介如梗狀，甚則咽腫喉痺。肝欬之狀，欬則兩脇下痛，甚則不可以轉，轉則兩胠下滿。脾欬之狀，欬則右脇下痛陰陰引肩背，甚則不可以動，動則欬劇。腎欬之狀，欬則腰背相引而痛，甚則欬涎。
		The Emperor asked: "How can these conditions be differentiated?" Qibo said: "The manifestation of lung cough is characterized by a cough with labored breathing and audible sounds; in severe cases, it may result in expectoration of blood." The manifestation of heart cough is that when one coughs, there is chest pain. In the throat there is a sensation as though something were lodged there; in severe cases this leads to swelling of the throat and hoarseness. The manifestation of liver cough is that when one coughs there is pain beneath the two flanks; in severe cases it becomes impossible to turn, and turning causes fullness beneath both sides. The manifestation of spleen cough is that when one coughs it causes pain beneath the right flank, with a dull sensation extending to the shoulders and back; in severe cases movement becomes impossible, and any movement intensifies the cough. The manifestation of kidney cough is that when one coughs the waist and back are drawn together in pain; in severe cases phlegm is expectorated.

3
打開字典
	欬論:	
帝曰：六府之欬柰何，安所受病。歧伯曰：五藏之久欬，乃移於六府，脾欬不已，則胃受之，胃欬之狀，欬而嘔，嘔甚則長蟲出。肝欬不已，則膽受之，膽欬之狀，欬嘔膽汁，肺欬不已，則大腸受之，大腸欬狀，欬而遺失。心欬不已，則小腸受之，小腸欬狀，欬而失氣，氣與欬俱失。腎欬不已，則膀胱受之，膀胱欬狀，欬而遺溺。久欬不已，則三焦受之，三焦欬狀，欬而腹滿，不欲食飲，此皆聚於胃，關於肺，使人多涕唾而面浮腫氣逆也。
		The Emperor asked: "What about coughing caused by the six fu organs? From where do they receive illness?" Qibo said: "When coughing from the five zang organs persists for a long time, it may transfer to the six fu organs. If spleen cough does not cease, then the stomach will be affected. The manifestation of stomach cough is characterized by cough with vomiting; in severe cases roundworms may be expelled." If liver cough does not cease, the gallbladder will be affected; the manifestation of gallbladder cough is characterized by coughing with vomiting of bile. If lung cough does not cease, it transfers to the large intestine; the manifestation of large intestine cough is characterized by cough accompanied by incontinence. If heart cough does not cease, then it transfers to the small intestine; the manifestation of small intestine cough is characterized by cough and loss of qi, with both qi and cough occurring simultaneously. If kidney cough does not cease, then it will transfer to the bladder; the manifestation of bladder cough is characterized by cough accompanied with involuntary urination. If coughing persists for a long time without ceasing, it will transfer to the triple burner. The manifestation of triple burner cough is characterized by cough with abdominal distension and lack of desire for food or drink. These conditions all converge in the stomach and are related to the lung, causing excessive nasal discharge, saliva production, facial swelling, and upward reversal of qi.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	欬論:	
帝曰：治之柰何。歧伯曰：治藏者，治其俞，治府者，治其合。浮腫者，治其經。帝曰：善。
		The Emperor asked: "How should these be treated?" Qibo said: "To treat the zang organs, treat their shu points; to treat the fu organs, treat their he points." For those with facial swelling, treat along the meridians. The Emperor said: "Good." 

39. 舉痛論 Treatise on Pain Syndromes

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	舉痛論:	
黃帝問曰：余聞善言天者，必有驗於人；善言古者，必有合於今；善言人者，必有厭於己。如此，則道不惑而要數極，所謂明也。今余問於夫子，令言而可知，視而可見，捫而可得，令驗於己而發蒙解惑，可得而聞乎。歧伯再拜稽首對曰：何道之問也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I have heard that those who speak well about Heaven must demonstrate proof in human affairs; Those who speak well about the past must find relevance in the present; Those who speak well about others must have insight into themselves. In this way, the Dao will not be confusing and its essential principles will reach their utmost, which is what is called clarity. Now I ask you, Master, to speak in a way that can be understood, seen upon observation, and grasped by touch, so that through personal verification it may dispel confusion and enlighten the ignorant—may I hear such instruction? Qibo bowed twice in deep respect and replied: What a profound inquiry about the Dao!

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	舉痛論:	
帝曰：願聞人之五藏卒痛，何氣使然。歧伯對曰：經脈流行不止，環周不休，寒氣入經而稽遲，泣而不行，客於脈外則血少，客於脈中則氣不通，故卒然而痛。
		The Emperor said: I wish to hear about sudden pain in the five zang organs of human beings—what kind of qi causes this? Qibo replied: The meridians circulate ceaselessly, flowing without pause. When cold qi enters the meridians and becomes stagnant, it causes stagnation and obstruction. If it lodges outside the vessels, blood becomes deficient; if it lodges inside the vessels, qi fails to pass through. Therefore, sudden pain occurs.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	舉痛論:	
帝曰：其痛或卒然而止者，或痛甚不休者，或痛甚不可按者，或按之而痛止者，或按之無益者，或喘動應手者，或心與背相引而痛者，或脇肋與少腹相引而痛者，或腹痛引陰股者，或痛宿昔而成積者，或卒然痛死不知人，有少閒復生者，或痛而嘔者，或腹痛而後泄者，或痛而閉不通者，凡此諸痛，各不同形，別之柰何。
		The Emperor said: Some pains suddenly cease, while others persist intensely without ceasing. Some are so severe they cannot be touched, whereas in other cases pressing on the area relieves the pain or has no effect at all. In some instances, there is a sensation of fluttering that responds to touch; in others, the chest and back pull against each other in pain, or the flanks and lower abdomen do likewise. Some experience abdominal pain radiating to the genitalia and thighs, while others develop palpable masses after prolonged pain. There are also cases where sudden severe pain causes unconsciousness, followed by a brief recovery; some vomit due to the pain, others have diarrhea following abdominal pain, or suffer from obstruction with no passage of stool or urine. All these various types of pain differ in their manifestations—how can they be distinguished?

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	舉痛論:	
歧伯曰：寒氣客於脈外，則脈寒，脈寒則縮踡，縮踡則脈絀急，則外引小絡，故卒然而痛，得炅則痛立止，因重中於寒，則痛久矣。寒氣客於經脈之中，與炅氣相薄，則脈滿，滿則痛而不可按也。寒氣稽留，炅氣從上，則脈充大而血氣亂，故痛甚不可按也。寒氣客於腸胃之閒，膜原之下，血不得散，小絡急引故痛，按之則血氣散，故按之痛止。寒氣客於俠脊之脈，則深按之不能及，故按之無益也。寒氣客於衝脈，衝脈起於關元，隨腹直上，寒氣客則脈不通，脈不通則氣因之，故喘動應手矣。寒氣客於背俞之脈則脈泣，脈泣則血虛，血虛則痛，其俞注於心，故相引而痛，按之則熱氣至，熱氣至則痛止矣。寒氣客於厥陰之脈，厥陰之脈者，絡陰器繫於肝，寒氣客於脈中，則血泣脈急，故脇肋與少腹相引痛矣。厥氣客於陰股，寒氣上及少腹，血泣在下相引，故腹痛引陰股。寒氣客於小腸膜原之間，絡血之中，血泣不得注於大經，血氣稽留不得行，故宿昔而成積矣。寒氣客於五藏，厥逆上泄，陰氣竭，陽氣未入，故卒然痛死不知人，氣復反，則生矣。寒氣客於腸胃，厥逆上出，故痛而嘔也。寒氣客於小腸，小腸不得成聚，故後泄腹痛矣。熱氣留於小腸，腸中痛，癉熱焦渴，則堅乾不得出，故痛而閉不通矣。帝曰：所謂言而可知者也。
		Qibo said: When cold qi lodges outside the vessels, it causes the vessels to become cold. Cold in the vessels leads them to contract and coil; when they contract and coil, the vessels constrict and tighten, pulling on the minor collateral vessels externally, hence sudden pain occurs. If warmth is applied, the pain stops immediately. However, if exposure to cold recurs, the pain will persist for a long time. When cold qi lodges within the meridians and collides with warm qi, it causes the vessels to fill. When they are filled, pain results and pressing on them becomes unbearable. When cold qi lingers and warm qi rises upward, the vessels become full and swollen while blood and qi become disordered; thus, the pain is severe and pressing on it becomes unbearable. When cold qi lodges between the intestines and stomach, beneath the serous membranes, blood cannot disperse; minor collateral vessels become taut and pull tightly, hence pain occurs. When pressed upon, the blood and qi disperse, so pressing relieves the pain. When cold qi lodges in the vessels along the spine, pressing deeply cannot reach it; therefore, pressing has no benefit. When cold qi lodges in the Chong Vessel, which originates at Guanyuan and ascends directly along the abdomen, the vessel becomes obstructed. When the vessel is blocked, qi follows suit and becomes stagnant; thus, a sensation of fluttering or shortness of breath occurs that responds to touch. When cold qi lodges in the back shu vessels, it causes stagnation of blood and fluids. When there is stagnation, blood becomes deficient, and deficiency leads to pain. The affected shu points are connected to the heart, so the chest and back pull on each other in pain; when pressed upon, warmth reaches the area, and with the arrival of warm qi, the pain ceases. When cold qi lodges in the Jueyin Vessel, which connects to the genital organs and is linked to the liver, if cold qi resides within the vessel, blood stagnates and the vessel becomes taut; thus, pain occurs with a pulling sensation between the flanks and lower abdomen. When cold qi lodges in the inner thighs, it ascends to affect the lower abdomen; blood stagnates below and is pulled upward, hence abdominal pain radiates to the inner thighs. When cold qi lodges between the small intestine and serous membranes, within the collateral vessels of blood, it causes stagnation so that blood cannot flow into the major meridians. Blood and qi remain stagnant without circulation; thus, over time, a mass forms. When cold qi lodges in the five zang organs, it causes reversal and upward discharge of qi. Yin energy is exhausted while Yang energy has not yet entered; thus sudden severe pain occurs with loss of consciousness. When the qi returns to normal, life is restored. When cold qi lodges in the intestines and stomach and causes reversal upward, pain with vomiting results. When cold qi lodges in the small intestine, it prevents the proper accumulation of food and fluids; thus diarrhea follows with abdominal pain. When heat qi lingers in the small intestine, pain arises within the intestines; dryness and heat cause thirst and scorching, making the stool hard and dry so that it cannot pass. Thus, pain with obstruction occurs. The Emperor said: This is what I meant by something that can be known through words.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	舉痛論:	
視而可見，柰何。歧伯曰：五藏六府，固盡有部，視其五色，黃赤為熱，白為寒，青黑為痛，此所謂視而可見者也。帝曰：捫而可得，柰何。歧伯曰：視其主病之脈堅而血，及陷下者，皆可捫而得也。
		What about something that can be seen upon observation? How so? Qibo said: The five zang and six fu organs each have their own specific locations. Observing the five colors—yellow and red indicate heat, white indicates cold, blue or black indicates pain—this is what is meant by something that can be seen upon observation. The Emperor said: What about something that can be grasped through palpation? How so? Qibo said: Examine the vessels associated with the disease—if they are firm and filled with blood, or sunken and deficient, all of these can be discerned through palpation.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	舉痛論:	
帝曰：善。余知百病生於氣也，怒則氣上，喜則氣緩，悲則氣消，恐則氣下，寒則氣收，炅則氣泄，驚則氣亂，勞則氣耗，思則氣結，九氣不同，何病之生。歧伯曰：怒則氣逆，甚則嘔血及飱泄，故氣上矣。喜則氣和志達，榮衛通利，故氣緩矣。悲則心系急，肺布葉舉，而上焦不通，榮衛不散，熱氣在中，故氣消矣。恐則精卻，卻則上焦閉，閉則氣還，還則下焦脹，故氣不行矣。寒則腠理閉，氣不行，故氣收矣。炅則腠理開，榮衛通汗大泄，故氣泄。驚則心無所倚，神無所歸，慮無所定，故氣亂矣。勞則喘息汗出，外內皆越，故氣耗矣。思則心有所存，神有所歸，正氣留而不行，故氣結矣。
		The Emperor said: Well said. I know that all kinds of illnesses arise from qi. Anger causes qi to rise, joy causes qi to relax, sorrow causes qi to dissipate, fear causes qi to descend, cold causes qi to contract, heat causes qi to disperse, startlement causes qi to become chaotic, overexertion causes qi to deplete, and worry causes qi to stagnate. These nine types of qi disturbances differ; what illnesses do they produce? Qibo said: Anger causes reversal of qi, and in severe cases it may lead to vomiting blood or dysentery; thus, qi rises. Joy harmonizes the qi and promotes mental clarity, allowing the nutritive and defensive qi to circulate smoothly; therefore, the qi becomes relaxed. Sorrow causes tension in the heart's connecting vessels, tightens the lung's leaf-like structures and obstructs the upper jiao, preventing the nutritive and defensive qi from dispersing. Heat accumulates internally; thus, the qi dissipates. Fear causes the essence to retreat inward; when this occurs, the upper jiao becomes closed. When it is closed, qi returns downward and accumulates in the lower jiao, causing distension; thus, the qi fails to circulate properly. Cold causes the pores of the skin to close and qi to stagnate; therefore, the qi contracts inwardly. Heat opens the pores, allowing nutritive and defensive qi to flow freely with profuse sweating; thus, the qi disperses outwardly. Startlement leaves the heart without support, the spirit without a place to reside, and thoughts unsettled; thus, the qi becomes chaotic. Overexertion causes panting, sweating, and excessive discharge of energy both internally and externally; therefore, the qi is depleted. Worry keeps the mind occupied and the spirit fixed in thought, causing righteous qi to remain stagnant rather than circulate; thus, the qi stagnates. 

40. 腹中論 Fuzhong Lun

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	腹中論:	
黃帝問曰：有病心腹滿，旦食則不能暮食，此為何病。歧伯對曰：名為鼓脹。帝曰：治之柰何。歧伯曰：治之以雞矢醴一劑知二劑已。帝曰：其時有復發者何也。歧伯曰：此飲食不節，故時有病也。雖然其病且已，時故當病，氣聚於腹也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "There is a disease in which the patient experiences fullness of the heart and abdomen, such that he cannot eat at dusk after eating in the morning. What kind of illness is this?" Qibo replied: "It is called guchang." The Emperor asked: "How should it be treated?" Qibo said: "It can be treated with Ji Shili. One dose will bring improvement, and two doses will cure it." The Emperor asked: "Why do some cases relapse at certain times?" Qibo said: "This is due to irregular diet, so the illness recurs from time to time." Although the disease may seem to be nearly cured, it will still cause illness at times because qi gathers in the abdomen.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	腹中論:	
帝曰：有病胸脇支滿者，妨於食，病至則先聞腥臊臭，出清液，先唾血，四支清，目眩，時時前後血，病名為何。何以得之。歧伯曰：病名血枯。此得之年少時，有所大脫血，若醉入房中，氣竭肝傷，故月事衰少不來也。帝曰：治之柰何，復以何術。歧伯曰：以四烏鰂骨一藘茹二物并合之，丸以雀卵，大如小豆，以五丸為後飯，飲以鮑魚汁，利腸中及傷肝也。
		The Emperor asked: "There is a disease in which a person experiences fullness and distension of the chest and flanks, which interferes with eating. When the illness occurs, the patient first smells foul odors such as fishy or putrid smells, discharges clear fluid, spits up blood first, has cold limbs, dizziness, and occasionally passes blood before and after urination. What is this disease called?" How does one contract it? Qibo said: "The disease is called xueku." This condition arises from when a person was young and experienced severe blood loss, or engaged in sexual activity while drunk. This leads to exhaustion of qi and injury to the liver, hence menstrual flow becomes weak and infrequent or stops altogether. The Emperor asked: "How should it be treated? What method can restore health?" Qibo said: "Combine four pieces of Wu Zigu and one piece of Lüru. Make pills with quail eggs, each as large as a small bean. Take five pills after meals, and drink abalone juice to benefit the intestines and heal liver injury."

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	腹中論:	
帝曰：病有少腹盛，上下左右皆有根，此為何病，可治不歧伯曰：病名曰伏梁。帝曰：伏梁何因而得之。歧伯曰：裹大膿血，居腸胃之外，不可治，治之，每切按之致死。帝曰：何以然。歧伯曰：此下則因陰必下膿血，上則迫胃脘生鬲，俠胃脘內癰。此久病也難治。居齊上為逆，居齊下為從，勿動亟奪，論在刺法中。帝曰：人有身體髀股䯒皆腫，環齊而痛，是為何病。歧伯曰：病名伏梁，此風根也。其氣溢於大腸而著於肓，肓之原在齊下，故環齊而痛也。不可動之，動之為水溺濇之病。
		The Emperor asked: "There is a disease in which there is fullness in the lower abdomen, with roots extending upward and downward as well as to both sides. What kind of illness is this? Is it curable?" Qibo said: "This disease is called Fuliang." The Emperor asked: "What causes the condition Fuliang?" Qibo said: "It is a mass of thick pus and blood, located outside the intestines and stomach. It cannot be treated. If one attempts to treat it by pressing or manipulating it forcefully, it may lead to death." The Emperor asked: "Why is that so?" Qibo said: "If it moves downward, it will cause pus and blood to discharge from the lower orifices; if it moves upward, it will press against the stomach region and produce a sensation of obstruction. It forms an internal abscess adjacent to the stomach." This is a long-standing illness and difficult to treat. If the condition resides above the navel, it is considered an inverse pattern; if below the navel, a following pattern. Do not manipulate or forcefully drain it. The discussion on this can be found in the methods of acupuncture. The Emperor asked: "There is a condition in which a person's body, thighs, hips, and calves are all swollen, with pain encircling the navel. What kind of disease is this?" Qibo said: "The disease is Fuliang; it is a root caused by wind." łoż Its qi overflows into the large intestine and lodges in the 肓 region. The origin of the 肓 is below the navel; hence, pain encircles the navel. It must not be manipulated forcefully; doing so will cause a condition with difficulty in urination and water retention.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	腹中論:	
帝曰：夫子數言熱中消中，不可服高梁芳草石藥，石藥發瘨，芳草發狂。夫熱中消中者皆富貴人也，今禁高梁，是不合其心，禁芳草石藥，是病不愈，願聞其說。歧伯曰：夫芳草之氣美，石藥之氣悍，二者其氣急疾堅勁，故非緩心和人，不可以服此二者。帝曰：不可以服此二者，何以然。歧伯曰：夫熱氣慓悍，藥氣亦然，二者相遇恐內傷脾。脾者土也而惡木，服此藥者，至甲乙日更論。
		The Emperor said: "You have often mentioned 'rezhong' and 'xiaozhong,' conditions in which one must not consume rich foods, fragrant herbs, or mineral medicines. Mineral medicines can cause delirium, while fragrant herbs may provoke mania." Those who suffer from rezhong and xiaozhong are usually wealthy and noble individuals. Now, if we prohibit them from eating rich foods, it goes against their desires; and if we forbid the use of fragrant herbs and mineral medicines, the illness will not be cured. I would like to hear your explanation. Qibo said: "The qi of fragrant herbs is pleasant, and the qi of mineral medicines is strong. Both are rapid, intense, and powerful in nature; therefore, they cannot be taken by those who lack a calm and harmonious mind." The Emperor asked: "Why can these two types of medicines not be used? What is the reason?" Qibo said: "The hot and aggressive nature of internal heat is similar to the strong qi of these medicines. When they meet, there is a risk of injuring the spleen internally." The spleen belongs to earth and is harmed by wood. Taking these medicines may cause further complications, especially on days corresponding to the heavenly stems Jia and Yi, which are associated with Wood. This should be considered carefully.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	腹中論:	
帝曰：善。有病膺腫頸痛胸滿腹脹此為何病，何以得之。歧伯曰：名厥逆。帝曰：治之柰何。歧伯曰：灸之則瘖，石之則狂，須其氣并，乃可治也。帝曰：何以然。歧伯曰：陽氣重上，有餘於上，灸之則陽氣入陰，入則瘖，石之則陽氣虛，虛則狂，須其氣并而治之，可使全也。
		The Emperor said: "Well said." There is a disease characterized by swelling of the chest, neck pain, fullness in the chest, and abdominal distension. What kind of illness is this, and how does one contract it? Qibo said: "It is called jue ni." The Emperor asked: "How should it be treated?" Qibo said: "If treated with moxibustion, the patient may become mute; if treated with acupuncture (stone therapy), they may go mad. One must wait until their qi is harmonized before treatment can be effective." The Emperor asked: "Why is that so?" Qibo said: "The yang qi rises excessively, accumulating in the upper part of the body. If treated with moxibustion (which further enhances yang), the excessive yang will enter the yin aspect; when it enters, the person may become mute. If acupuncture is used to drain the excess, the yang qi becomes deficient, and deficiency can lead to mania. One must wait until the qi has harmonized before treatment, which can then restore full health."

打開字典
	腹中論:	
帝曰：善。何以知懷子之且生也。歧伯曰：身有病而無邪脈也。
		The Emperor said: "Well said." How can one know that a pregnant woman is about to give birth? Qibo said: "The body shows symptoms of illness, but there is no pathological pulse."

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	腹中論:	
帝曰：病熱而有所痛者，何也。歧伯曰：病熱者，陽脈也，以三陽之動也，人迎一盛少陽，二盛太陽，三盛陽明，入陰也，夫陽入於陰，故病在頭與腹，乃䐜脹而頭痛也。帝曰：善。
		The Emperor asked: "What causes a person to have fever and pain?" Qibo said: "A feverish condition is characterized by yang pulses, which reflect the movement of the three yang meridians. When the Renying pulse is one and a half times stronger than normal, it indicates involvement of the Shaoyang; two and a half times indicates Taiyang; and three and a half times indicates Yangming. This signifies that yang has entered the yin aspect. When yang enters into yin, illness occurs in the head and abdomen, resulting in distension and headache." The Emperor said: "Well said." 

41. 刺腰痛 Treating Low Back Pain

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺腰痛:	
足太陽脈，令人腰痛，引項脊尻背如重狀；刺其郄中太陽正經出血，春無見血。少陽令人腰痛，如以鍼刺其皮中，循循然不可以俛仰，不可以顧。刺少陽成骨之端出血，成骨在膝外廉之骨獨起者，夏無見血。陽明令人腰痛，不可以顧顧如有見者，善悲；刺陽明於䯒前三痏，上下和之出血。秋無見血，足少陰令人腰痛，痛引脊內廉；刺少陰於內踝上二痏，春無見血，出血太多，不可復也。厥陰之脈，令人腰痛，腰中如張弓弩弦；刺厥陰之脈，在腨踵魚腹之外，循之累累然，乃刺之，其病令人善言，默默然不慧，刺之三痏。解脈令人腰痛，痛引肩，目䀮䀮然，時遺溲；刺解脈，在膝筋肉分閒郄外廉之橫脈出血，血變而止。解脈令人腰痛如引帶，常如折腰狀，善恐，刺解脈，在郄中結絡如黍米，刺之血射以黑，見赤血而已。同陰之脈，令人腰痛，痛如小錘居其中，怫然腫；刺同陰之脈，在外踝上絕骨之端，為三痏。陽維之脈，令人腰痛，痛上怫然腫，刺陽維之脈，脈與太陽合腨下間，去地一尺所。衡絡之脈，令人腰痛，不可以俛仰，仰則恐仆，得之舉重傷腰，衡絡絕，惡血歸之；刺之在郄陽筋之間，上郄數寸，衡居為二痏出血。會陰之脈，令人腰痛，痛上漯漯然汗出，汗乾令人欲飲，飲已欲走。刺直陽之脈，上三痏在蹻上郄下五寸橫居，視其盛者出血。飛陽之脈，令人腰痛，痛上拂拂然，甚則悲以恐；刺飛陽之脈，在內踝上五寸，少陰之前，與陰維之會。昌陽之脈，令人腰痛，痛引膺，目䀮䀮然，甚則反折，舌卷不能言；刺內筋為二痏，在內踝上大筋前，太陰後上踝二寸所。散脈令人腰痛而熱，熱甚生煩，腰下如有橫木居其中，甚則遺溲，刺散脈，在膝前骨肉分間，絡外廉束脈為三痏。肉里之脈，令人腰痛，不可以欬，欬則筋縮急，刺肉里之脈為二痏，在太陽之外，少陽絕骨之後。
		The Bladder Meridian of Foot-Taiyang causes lower back pain and draws pain to the neck, spine, buttocks, and upper back as if bearing a heavy weight. Needling its collateral at the Taiyang main meridian draws blood; in spring, avoid drawing blood. The Triple Energizer Meridian causes lower back pain as if a needle were piercing the skin, with a gradual sensation that prevents one from bending forward or backward and makes it impossible to turn the head. Needling the end of the chenggu bone along the Shaoyang meridian draws blood; the chenggu is the prominent bone on the outer side of the knee. In summer, avoid drawing blood. The Yangming Meridian causes lower back pain that prevents one from turning to look around as if seeing something, and it often brings about sadness. Needle the Yangming at three points in front of the yu, drawing blood both above and below to harmonize them. Avoid drawing blood in autumn; the Kidney Meridian of Foot-Shaoyin causes lower back pain, with pain extending along the inner side of the spine. Needle the Shaoyin at two points above the medial malleolus; avoid drawing blood in spring, as excessive bleeding cannot be restored. The Liver Meridian of Foot-Jueyin causes lower back pain with a sensation as if the waist were stretched like a bowstring. Needle the Jueyin meridian at points on the outer side of the calf and behind the heel, where one can feel a series of lumps; this condition causes excessive talking and makes the person quiet and unresponsive. Needling should be done at three points. The Jie meridian causes lower back pain, which draws to the shoulder and results in a dimming vision and occasional urinary incontinence. Needle the Jie meridian at the horizontal vessel on the outer side of the gap between the knee tendons and muscles, drawing blood until its color changes. The Jie meridian causes back pain as if being pulled by a belt, often feeling like the waist is broken, and brings about frequent fear. To needle the Jie meridian, locate the knotted collateral in the gap resembling millet grains; when needled, black blood should be expelled until red blood appears. The Tongyin meridian causes lower back pain with the sensation of a small hammer resting inside, causing sudden swelling. Needle the Tongyin meridian at three points on the end of the prominent bone above the lateral malleolus. The Yangwei meridian causes lower back pain accompanied by sudden swelling upward. To needle the Yangwei meridian, locate it where it converges with the Taiyang in the area beneath the calf, about one chi above ground level. The Hengluo meridian causes lower back pain so severe that one cannot bend forward or backward; when attempting to look upward, there is a fear of falling. This condition results from lifting heavy objects and injuring the waist, causing the Hengluo collateral to be severed and stagnant blood to accumulate there. Needle it between the Yang tendons at a point several cun above the gap, where two points are horizontally situated to draw blood. The Huiyin meridian causes lower back pains, with a sensation of dripping sweat upwards. Once the sweat dries, one feels thirsty; after drinking, there is an urge to run. Needle the Zhiyang meridian at three points located five cun below and horizontally situated from the qiao point above the gap; select the most prominent vessel to draw blood. The Feiyang meridian causes lower back pain which spreads upward, and in severe cases brings about sadness and fear. Needle the Feiyang meridian at five cun above the medial malleolus, in front of the Shaoyin and where it converges with the Yinyue meridian. The Changyang meridian causes lower back pain drawing to the chest, with dimming vision; in severe cases, it results in spinal stiffness and tongue retraction that prevents speech. Needle the inner tendons at two points, located on the front of the large tendon above the medial malleolus and behind the Taiyin, about two cun above the ankle. The San meridian causes lower back pain along with heat; excessive heat brings about restlessness, and there is a sensation as if a horizontal piece of wood were lodged in the waist. In severe cases, urinary incontinence occurs. To needle the San meridian, select three points on the lateral side of the collateral between the bone and flesh at the front of the knee. The Rouli meridian causes lower back pain to such an extent that one cannot cough; coughing causes the tendons to contract and tighten. To needle the Rouli meridian, use two points located outside the Taiyang and behind the prominent bone of the Shaoyang.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺腰痛:	
腰痛俠脊而痛至頭几几然，目䀮䀮欲僵仆；刺足太陽郄中出血，腰痛上寒，刺足太陽陽明；上熱，刺足厥陰。不可以俛仰，刺足少陽。中熱而喘，刺足少陰，刺郄中出血。腰痛上寒不可顧，刺足陽明；上熱，刺足太陰，中熱而喘，刺足少陰。大便難刺足少陰。少腹滿，刺足厥陰。如折不可以俛仰不可舉，刺足太陽。引脊內廉，刺足少陰。腰痛引少腹控䏚，不可以仰，刺腰尻交者，兩髁胂上。以月生死為痏數，發鍼立已，左取右，右取左。
		Lower back pain extends along the spine to the head, causing a stiff and rigid sensation; vision becomes dim, with an urge to collapse. Needle the gap of the Foot-Taiyang meridian to draw blood. For lower back pain accompanied by cold upward, needle the Foot-Taiyang and Yangming meridians. For upper heat, needle the Foot-Jueyin meridian.
