If one cannot bend forward or backward, needle the Foot-Shaoyang meridian. For central heat and shortness of breath, needle the Foot-Shaoyin meridian and draw blood from its gap. Lower back pain with upward cold preventing one from turning around should be treated by needling the Foot-Yangming meridian; For upper heat, needle the Spleen Meridian of Foot-Taiyin. For central heat and shortness of breath ， needle the Kidney Meridian of the Foot-Shaoyin. For difficulty in passing stools, needle the Kidney Meridian (Foot-Shaoyin). For distension of the lower abdomen, needle the Liver Meridian (Foot-Jueyin). If it feels as if broken and one cannot bend forward or backward or lift up, needle the Bladder Meridian (Foot-Taiyang). For pain drawing along the inner side of the spinal column, needle the Kidney Meridians (Foot-Shaoyin) Lower back pain radiating to the lower abdomen and causing difficulty in breathing, making it impossible to look upward; needle at the intersection of the waist and buttocks, above the two trochanters. Determine the number of acupuncture points based on the waxing or waning phase of the moon; insert the needle and the condition should be immediately relieved. If symptoms are on the left, treat the right side; if on the right, treat the left side. 

42. 風論 Treatise on Wind

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	風論:	
黃帝問曰：風之傷人也，或為寒熱，或為熱中，或為寒中，或為癘風，或為偏枯，或為風也；其病各異，其名不同，或內至五藏六府，不知其解，願聞其說。歧伯對曰：風氣藏於皮膚之閒，內不得通，外不得泄；風者，善行而數變，腠理開則洒然寒，閉則熱而悶，其寒也，則衰食飲，其熱也，則消肌肉，故使人怢慄而不能食，名曰寒熱。風氣與陽明入胃循脈而上至目內眥，其人肥則風氣不得外泄，則為熱中而目黃；人瘦，則外泄而寒，則為寒中而泣出。風氣與太陽俱入行諸脈俞，散於分肉之閒，與衛氣相干，其道不利，故使肌肉憤䐜而有瘍；衛氣有所凝而不行，故其肉有不仁也。癘者有榮氣熱胕，其氣不清，故使其鼻柱壞而色敗，皮膚瘍潰。風寒客於脈而不去，名曰癘風，或名曰寒熱。
		The Yellow Emperor asked, "When wind harms people, it may cause cold and heat, or internal heat, or internal cold, or leprosy-like wind, or hemiplegia, or simply wind." Their symptoms are different and their names vary; some penetrate inward to the five zang and six fu organs. I do not understand how this occurs. I wish to hear your explanation. Qibo replied, "Wind qi lodges between the skin and flesh; internally it cannot circulate, externally it cannot be discharged;" "Wind is characterized by its rapid movement and frequent changes. When the pores open, it causes a sudden chill; when they close, it produces heat and oppression. In cases of cold, one loses appetite for food and drink; in cases of heat, body tissues are consumed. Thus, it makes people shiver and lose their ability to eat, which is called cold-heat." When wind qi combines with Yangming and enters the stomach, it follows the meridians upward to the inner canthus of the eye. In a fat person, wind qi cannot be expelled outward, resulting in internal heat and yellowing of the eyes; In a thin person, it is discharged externally as cold, leading to internal cold with tears flowing out. "When wind qi combines with Taiyang and enters, it travels through the collateral channels, spreading between the muscle layers, where it interacts with wei qi. When its passage is obstructed, it causes swelling of the muscles and formation of sores;" "wei qi becomes stagnant and cannot circulate properly, hence there is numbness in the affected flesh." "Leprosy-like conditions arise when nutritive qi is excessively hot, and the qi is impure; thus it causes destruction of the nasal bridge, discoloration of the complexion, and ulceration of the skin." "When wind-cold lodges in the meridians and does not depart, it is called leprosy-like wind or alternatively cold-heat."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	風論:	
以春甲乙傷於風者為肝風；以夏丙丁傷於風者為心風；以以季夏戊己傷於邪者為脾風；秋庚辛中於邪者為肺風；以冬壬癸中於邪者為腎風。風中五藏六府之俞亦為藏府之風，各入其門戶所中，則為偏風。風氣循風府而上，則為腦風；風入係頭，則為目風眼寒；飲酒中風，則為漏風；入房汗出中風，則為內風；新沐中風則為首風；久風入中，則為腸風飱泄；外在腠理，則為泄風。故風者，百病之長也，至其變化，乃為他病也，無常方，然致有風氣也。
		Those injured by wind during spring on days Jia or Yi are said to have liver wind; those injured by wind in summer on days Bing or Ding are said to have heart wind; those injured by pathogenic factors during the late summer on days Wu or Ji are said to have spleen wind; lord: those injured by pathogenic influences in autumn on days Geng or Xin are said to have lung wind; Those affected by pathogenic factors in winter on days Ren or Gui are said to have kidney wind. "When wind strikes the shu points of the five zang and six fu, it is also called wind affecting the zang-fu organs. Depending on which meridian gate or location is struck, it becomes hemiplegia." "When wind qi follows the Fengfu point upward, it results in brain wind;" "when wind enters the head region, it causes eye wind and cold eyes;" "drinking alcohol while catching wind results in leaking wind;" "if one catches wind after perspiring during sexual activity, it becomes internal wind;" "catching wind immediately after a bath results in head wind;" "when long-standing wind enters inwardly, it becomes intestinal wind and causes diarrhea with undigested food;" "When located externally in the pores and skin layers, it is called leaking wind." Therefore, wind is the leader of all diseases. When it changes and transforms, it gives rise to other illnesses. It has no fixed pattern, yet wherever there are symptoms, there is also wind qi.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	風論:	
帝曰：五藏風之形狀不同者何，願聞其診及其病能。歧伯曰：肺風之狀，多汗惡風，色皏然白，時欬短氣，晝日則差，暮則甚，診在眉上，其色白；心風之狀，多汗惡風，焦絕善怒嚇，赤色，病甚則言不可快，診在口，其色赤；肝風之狀，多汗惡風，善悲色微蒼，嗌乾善怒，時憎女子，診在目下，其色青；脾風之狀，多汗惡風，身體怠墯，四支不欲動，色薄微黃，不嗜食，診在鼻上，其色黃；腎風之狀，多汗惡風，面痝然浮腫，脊痛不能正立，其色炱，隱曲不利，診在肌上，其色黑，胃風之狀，頸多汗惡風，食飲不下，鬲塞不通，腹善滿，失衣則䐜脹，食寒則泄，診形瘦而腹大；首風之狀頭面多汗惡風當先風一日，則病甚頭痛不可，以出內至其風日，則病少愈；漏風之狀，或多汗，常不可單衣，食則汗出，甚則身汗，喘息惡風，衣常濡，口乾善渴，不能勞事；泄風之狀，多汗，汗出泄衣上，口中乾上漬，其風不能勞事，身體盡痛則寒。帝曰：善。
		The Emperor asked, "What are the different manifestations of wind affecting the five zang organs? I wish to hear about their diagnosis and the nature of their diseases." Qibo said, "The manifestation of lung wind is profuse sweating and aversion to wind. The complexion appears pale yellowish-white, with frequent coughing and shortness of breath. Symptoms are milder during the day but worse at night. Diagnosis lies above the eyebrows; its color is white;" "The manifestation of heart wind includes profuse sweating, aversion to wind, irritability and sudden outbursts of anger or fear, a reddish complexion. In severe cases, speech becomes difficult and incoherent. Diagnosis is at the mouth; its color is red;" "The manifestation of liver wind includes profuse sweating and aversion to cold, a tendency toward sadness with a slightly bluish complexion, dry throat, frequent anger, occasional dislike for women. Diagnosis is below the eyes; its color is green;" "The manifestation of spleen wind includes profuse sweating and sensitivity to cold, general fatigue, reluctance to move the limbs, a pale yellow complexion, lack of appetite. Diagnosis is on the nose; its color is yellow;" "The manifestation of kidney wind includes profuse sweating and cold sensitivity, facial puffiness and swelling, back pain preventing upright posture, a complexion like soot, discomfort in the lower abdomen with obstruction. Diagnosis is on the muscles; its color is black. The manifestation of stomach wind includes excessive sweating at the neck and aversion to wind, inability to swallow food or drink, blockage behind the sternum, frequent abdominal distension, swelling when undressed, diarrhea after consuming cold foods. Diagnosis shows a thin body with an enlarged abdomen;" "The manifestation of head wind includes excessive sweating on the head and face, aversion to cold. Symptoms worsen one day before a wind event, causing severe headache that cannot be relieved; by the time the wind arrives, symptoms slightly improve;" "The manifestation of leaking wind includes excessive sweating, often making it impossible to wear a single layer of clothing. Sweating occurs after eating; in severe cases, the whole body sweats, with shortness of breath and aversion to cold. Clothing is constantly damp, the mouth is dry, there is frequent thirst, and one cannot perform physical labor;" "The manifestation of leaking wind is profuse sweating that soaks through clothing, dryness in the mouth with a sticky sensation. Those affected by this type of wind cannot perform physical labor and experience whole-body pain along with chills." The Emperor said, "Well said." 

43. 痺論 Bi Lun

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	痺論:	
黃帝問曰：痺之安生。歧伯對曰：風寒濕三氣雜至，合而為痺也。其風氣勝者為行痺，寒氣勝者為痛痺，濕氣勝者為著痺也。帝曰：其有五者，何也。歧伯曰：以冬遇此者為骨痺，以春遇此者為筋痺，以夏遇此者為脈痺，以至陰遇此者為肌痺，以秋遇此者為皮痺。帝曰：內舍五藏六府，何氣使然。歧伯曰：五藏皆有合病，久而不去者，內舍於其合也。故骨痺不已，復感於邪，內舍於腎；筋痺不已，復感於邪內舍於肝；脈痺不已，復感於邪內舍於心；肌痺不已，復感於邪內舍於脾；皮痺不已，復感於邪，內舍於肺，所謂痺者，各以其時，重感於風寒濕之氣也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "How does bi syndrome arise?" Qi Bo replied: "Wind, cold, and dampness are three pathogenic factors that combine to cause bi syndrome." When the wind factor predominates, it causes traveling bi; when cold predominates, it causes painful bi; and when dampness predominates, it causes adhering bi. The Emperor asked: "Why are there five types of bi?" Qi Bo said: "When one encounters this in winter, it is bone bi; when encountered in spring, it is tendon bi; when encountered in summer, it is vessel bi; when encountered during the yin period (late summer), it is muscle bi; and when encountered in autumn, it is skin bi." The Emperor asked: "When this condition resides internally within the five zang and six fu organs, what pathogenic factors cause such a situation?" Qi Bo said: "All the five zang organs can be affected by combined diseases. If the condition persists without resolution, it will internally reside in its corresponding paired organ." Therefore, if bone bi does not resolve and is again affected by pathogenic factors, it resides internally in the kidney; if tendon bi does not resolve and one is again affected by external pathogens, it will reside internally in the liver; if vessel bi does not resolve and there is a further invasion of pathogenic factors, it will reside internally within the heart; if muscle bi does not resolve and external pathogens invade again, it resides internally in the spleen; if skin bi does not resolve and pathogenic factors invade again, it resides internally within the lungs. What is referred to as bi syndrome arises when, during its respective season, one is repeatedly affected by wind, cold, and damp pathogenic qi.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	痺論:	
凡痺之客五藏者，肺痺者，煩滿喘而嘔；心痺者，脈不通，煩則心下鼓，暴上氣而喘，嗌乾善噫，厥氣上則恐；肝痺者，夜臥則驚，多飲數小便，上為引如懷；腎痺者，善脹，尻以代踵，脊以代頭；脾痺者，四支解墯，發欬嘔汁，上為大塞；腸痺者，數飲而出不得，中氣喘爭，時發飱泄；胞痺者，少腹膀胱，按之內痛，若沃以湯，澀於小便，上為清涕。
		Whenever bi syndrome affects the five zang organs, those with lung bi experience irritability, fullness in the chest, shortness of breath, and vomiting; those with heart bi experience obstruction in the vessels. When agitated, they feel a distending sensation beneath the heart, sudden upward rushing of qi causing shortness of breath and panting, dry throat, frequent belching, and when rebellious qi rises upward, they become fearful; those with liver bi experience startlement during sleep at night, excessive drinking and frequent urination, and a sensation of pulling upward in the upper abdomen as if something were held there; those with kidney bi tend to have abdominal distension, sit on their buttocks instead of standing on their heels, and arch the spine as if it were the head; those with spleen bi experience weakness and heaviness in all four limbs, coughing, vomiting of fluids, and obstruction in the upper thorax; those with intestinal bi experience frequent drinking but are unable to urinate, internal qi rushing upward causing panting and occasional episodes of diarrhea; those with bladder bi experience pain in the lower abdomen and bladder upon palpation, as if scalded by hot water. They suffer from difficulty urinating and clear nasal discharge.

3
打開字典
	痺論:	
陰氣者，靜則神藏，躁則消亡。飲食自倍，腸胃乃傷。淫氣喘息，痺聚在肺；淫氣憂思，痺聚在心；淫氣遺溺，痺聚在腎；淫氣乏竭，痺聚在肝；淫氣肌絕，痺聚在脾。諸痺不已，亦益內也，其風氣勝者，其人易已也。帝曰：痺，其時有死者，或疼久者，或易已者，其故何也。歧伯曰：其入藏者死，其留連筋骨閒者疼久，其留皮膚閒者易已。
		Yin qi is such that when it remains tranquil, the spirit is stored; but if disturbed or agitated, it dissipates and is lost. Excessive consumption of food and drink doubles one's intake, thereby injuring the intestines and stomach. Excess pathogenic qi causing panting and labored breathing leads to bi syndrome accumulating in the lungs; excessive pathogenic qi arising from worry and overthinking causes bi syndrome to accumulate in the heart; excessive pathogenic qi leading to urinary dribbling causes bi syndrome to gather in the kidneys; exhaustion of vital energy due to excessive pathogenic influences leads to bi syndrome gathering in the liver; pathogenic qi damaging the muscles causes bi syndrome to accumulate in the spleen. If various forms of bi syndrome persist without resolution, they will progressively affect the interior. However, if wind pathogenic factor predominates in a case, such individuals tend to recover more easily. The Emperor asked: "In bi syndrome, some cases result in death at a certain time, others experience prolonged pain, and still others resolve easily. What is the reason for this?" Qi Bo said: "Those in which bi syndrome enters the zang organs are fatal; those that remain between the tendons and bones cause prolonged pain; and those that linger within the skin resolve easily."

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	痺論:	
帝曰：其客於六府者何也。歧伯曰：此亦其食飲居處，為其病本也。六府亦各有俞，風寒濕氣中其俞，而食飲應之，循俞而入，各舍其府也。帝曰：以鍼治之，柰何。歧伯曰：五藏有俞，六府有合，循脈之分，各有所發，各隨其過，則病瘳也。
		The Emperor asked: "What is it like when bi syndrome affects the six fu organs?" Qi Bo said: "This also originates from one's dietary habits, lifestyle, and living conditions, which form the root cause of the disease." The six fu organs also each have their respective shu points. When wind, cold, or damp pathogenic qi enters through these shu points and is further exacerbated by improper diet and drink, the pathogens follow the pathways of the shu points inward and settle in their corresponding fu organs. The Emperor asked: "How should acupuncture be used to treat this?" Qi Bo said: "The five zang organs have shu points, and the six fu organs have he points. Following the distribution of meridians, each has its own location for manifestation. Treatment should follow the nature of the excess or deficiency in each case; then the illness will resolve."

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	痺論:	
帝曰：榮衛之氣，亦令人痺乎。歧伯曰：榮者水穀之精氣也，和調於五藏，灑陳於六府，乃能入於脈也；故循脈上下，貫五藏絡六府也。衛者水穀之悍氣也，其氣慓疾滑利，不能入於脈也；故循皮膚之中，分肉之閒，熏於肓膜，散於胸腹，逆其氣則病，從其氣則愈。不與風寒濕氣合，故不為痺。帝曰：善。痺或痛，或不痛或不仁，或寒，或熱，或燥，或濕，其故何也。歧伯曰：痛者，寒氣多也，有寒故痛也。其不痛不仁者，病久入深，榮衛之行濇，經絡時踈，故不通，皮膚不營，故為不仁。其寒者，陽氣少，陰氣多，與病相益，故寒也；其熱者，陽氣多，陰氣少，病氣勝陽遭陰，故為痺熱。其多汗而濡者，此其逢濕甚也，陽氣少，陰氣盛，兩氣相感，故汗出而濡也。帝曰：夫痺之為病，不痛何也。歧伯曰：痺在於骨則重，在於脈則血凝而不流，在於筋則屈不伸，在於肉則不仁，在於皮則寒，故具此五者，則不痛也。凡痺之類，逢寒則蟲，逢熱則縱。帝曰：善。
		The Emperor asked: "Can the qi of ying and wei also cause bi syndrome?" Qi Bo said: "Ying qi is the refined essence of water and grains. It harmonizes with the five zang organs and distributes to the six fu organs before it can enter the vessels; thus, it circulates up and down along the vessels, connecting the five zang organs and linking with the six fu organs. Wei qi is the vigorous and swift-moving aspect of water and grain essence; it is rapid, smooth, and slippery in nature, and cannot enter the vessels; therefore, it circulates within the skin and between muscle layers, permeates the pericardial membrane, and disperses in the thorax and abdomen. If its movement is obstructed or reversed, disease occurs; if its flow remains harmonious, recovery follows. If it does not combine with wind, cold, and damp pathogens, then bi syndrome will not occur. The Emperor said: "Well said." Bi syndrome may present as pain, or no pain; as numbness, or cold sensation, or heat, or dryness, or dampness. What is the cause of these differences? Qi Bo said: "Pain occurs when cold pathogenic qi is predominant; pain arises because of the presence of cold." Those who experience neither pain nor numbness have a condition that has persisted for a long time and deeply invaded the body. The ying and wei qi flow becomes sluggish, meridians become intermittently obstructed, hence there is no communication; the skin and flesh are not nourished, thus resulting in numbness or insensitivity. Those who feel cold do so because their yang qi is deficient and yin qi is excessive; this imbalance exacerbates the condition, hence the sensation of cold. Those who feel heat do so because their yang qi is excessive and yin qi is deficient; the pathogenic factors overpower the yang, which then encounters the deficiency of yin, hence resulting in bi syndrome with a sensation of heat. Those who perspire profusely and feel dampness do so because they have encountered excessive damp pathogenic factors. In such cases, yang qi is deficient while yin qi is abundant; the interaction between these two types of qi results in sweating and a sensation of dampness. The Emperor asked: "In bi syndrome, why does one sometimes experience no pain?" Qi Bo said: "When bi syndrome affects the bones, it causes heaviness; when it affects the vessels, blood becomes stagnant and does not flow; when it affects the tendons, they become bent and unable to stretch; when it affects the muscles, numbness occurs; and when it affects the skin, cold is felt. Therefore, if all these five conditions are present together, there will be no pain." In general, bi syndrome of any type tends to become more contracted when encountering cold and more relaxed or dispersed when encountering heat. The Emperor said: "Well said." 

44. 痿論 Wei Lun (Treatise on Atrophy)

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	痿論:	
黃帝問曰：五藏使人痿，何也。歧伯對曰：肺主身之皮毛，心主身之血脈，肝主身之筋膜，脾主身之肌肉，腎主身之骨髓。故肺熱葉焦，則皮毛虛弱，急薄著則生痿躄也；心氣熱，則下脈厥而上，上則下脈虛，虛則生脈痿，樞折挈，脛縱而不任地也；肝氣熱，則膽泄口苦筋膜乾，筋膜乾則筋急而攣，發為筋痿；脾氣熱，則胃乾而渴，肌肉不仁，發為肉痿；腎氣熱，則腰脊不舉，骨枯而髓減，發為骨痿。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "Why do the five zang organs cause atrophy?" Qibo replied: "The lungs govern the skin and hair of the body, the heart governs the blood vessels, the liver governs the tendons and connective tissue, the spleen governs the muscles, and the kidneys govern the bones and bone marrow." Therefore, when heat in the lungs causes the lung leaves to wither, the skin and hair become weak. If this weakness becomes severe and thin, it leads to atrophy and lameness. When the heart qi becomes hot, it causes the lower blood vessels to become inverted and move upward. When this occurs, the lower vessels become deficient; deficiency leads to atrophy of the vessels (maiwei), causing the pivot joint to collapse and drag, resulting in the shins becoming loose and unable to bear weight on the ground. When the liver qi becomes hot, the gallbladder discharges excessively, causing a bitter taste in the mouth and dryness of the tendons and connective tissues. When the tendons and connective tissues become dry, the tendons tighten and contract, leading to atrophy of the tendons (jinwei). When the spleen qi becomes hot, the stomach dries up and thirst occurs; the muscles lose sensation, leading to atrophy of the flesh (rouwei). When the kidney qi becomes hot, the waist and spine cannot be raised; the bones become dry and the bone marrow decreases, leading to atrophy of the bones (guwei).

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	痿論:	
帝曰：何以得之。歧伯曰：肺者藏之長也，為心之蓋也；有所失亡，所求不得，則發肺鳴，鳴則肺熱葉焦，故曰：五藏因肺熱葉焦，發為痿躄，此之謂也。悲哀太甚，則胞絡絕，胞絡絕，則陽氣內動，發則心下崩數溲血也。故《本病》曰：大經空虛，發為肌痺，傳為脈痿。思想無窮，所願不得，意淫於外，入房太甚，宗筋弛縱，發為筋痿，及為白淫。故《下經》曰：筋痿者，生於肝使內也。有漸於濕以水為事，若有所留居處相濕，肌肉濡漬，痺而不仁，發為肉痿。故《下經》曰：肉痿者，得之濕地也。有所遠行勞倦，逢大熱而渴，渴則陽氣內伐，內伐則熱舍於腎，腎者，水藏也，今水不勝火，則骨枯而髓虛，故足不任身，發為骨痿。故《下經》曰，骨痿者，生於大熱也。帝曰：何以別之。歧伯曰：肺熱者，色白而毛敗；心熱者，色赤而絡脈溢，肝熱者，色蒼而爪枯；脾熱者，色黃而肉蠕動；腎熱者，色黑而齒槁。
		The Emperor asked: "How does one acquire this?" Qibo said: "The lungs are the superior organ among the zang organs, and serve as a cover for the heart;" "When one suffers loss or fails to obtain what is desired, it causes the lungs to resonate; when they resonate, lung heat arises and the lung leaves wither. Hence it is said: 'The five zang organs become atrophic due to lung heat causing the lung leaves to wither,' this is what that means." Excessive sorrow and grief sever the bladder vessels; when the bladder vessels are severed, internal yang qi becomes disturbed. When this occurs, there is sudden collapse beneath the heart with frequent urination of blood. Hence it is said in Benbing: "When the great meridians become empty and deficient, it manifests as muscle numbness (jibi), which progresses to atrophy of the vessels." Endless brooding and unfulfilled desires cause one's thoughts to be indulgent outwardly; excessive sexual activity causes the seminal tendons to relax and loosen, leading to atrophy of the sinews (jinwei), as well as to leukorrhea. Hence it is said in Xiaojing: "Atrophy of the sinews arises from dysfunction within the liver." Those who are gradually affected by dampness due to constant contact with water, or those who reside in a place where dampness lingers, will have their muscles soaked and become numb without sensation, leading to atrophy of flesh (rouwei). Hence it is said in Xiaojing: "Atrophy of the flesh arises from dwelling in damp places." When one travels far and becomes exhausted, encountering intense heat which causes thirst; this thirst leads to internal depletion of yang qi. When yang is depleted, the heat lodges in the kidneys. The kidneys are the organ that stores water; now, water cannot overcome fire, so the bones dry up and bone marrow becomes deficient. Hence, the feet can no longer support the body, leading to atrophy of bone (guwei). Hence it is said in Xiaojing: "Atrophy of the bones arises from intense heat." The Emperor asked: "How can one distinguish them?" Qibo said: "Those with lung heat have a pale complexion and poor hair." Those with heart heat have a red complexion and overflowing collateral vessels; those with liver heat have a bluish-green complexion and dry nails. Those with spleen heat have a yellow complexion and trembling muscles; those with kidney heat have a dark complexion and dry teeth.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	痿論:	
帝曰：如夫子言可矣，論言治痿者獨取陽明何也。歧伯曰：陽明者，五藏六府之海，主閏宗筋，宗筋主束骨而利機關也。衝脈者經脈之海也，主滲灌谿谷，與陽明合於宗筋，陰陽揔宗筋之會，會於氣街，而陽明為之長，皆屬於帶脈，而絡於督脈。故陽明虛則宗筋縱，帶脈不引，故足痿不用也。帝曰：治之柰何。歧伯曰：各補其滎而通其俞，調其虛實，和其逆順，筋脈骨肉，各以其時受月，則病已矣。帝曰：善。
		The Emperor said: "What you say is acceptable, but the classical text states that in treating atrophy one should exclusively take the Yangming meridian—why is this so?" Qibo said: "The Yangming meridian is the sea of the five zang and six fu organs, governing the seminal tendons. The seminal tendons govern the binding of bones and the smooth functioning of joints." The Chong meridian is the sea of all the meridians, governing the seepage and irrigation of the xigou (meridian channels). It converges with the Yangming at the seminal tendons; yin and yang converge at this junction. This convergence occurs at the qijie (energy crossroads), where the Yangming serves as the leader. All these meridians belong to the Dai meridian and connect to the Du meridian. Therefore, when the Yangming becomes deficient, the seminal tendons relax, the Dai meridian fails to restrain them, and thus the feet become atrophied and nonfunctional. The Emperor asked: "How should this be treated?" Qibo said: "Strengthen the 荥 (Xing) points of each affected organ and unblock their Shu points. Regulate deficiency and excess, harmonize the flow in accordance with its natural order. The tendons, vessels, bones, and muscles will then respond according to their respective seasons and lunar phases; when this is achieved, the disease will be resolved." The Emperor said: "Good." 

45. 厥論 Jue Lun

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	厥論:	
黃帝問曰：厥之寒熱者何也。歧伯對曰：陽氣衰於下，則為寒厥；陰氣衰於下，則為熱厥。帝曰：熱厥之為熱也，必起於足下者，何也。歧伯曰：陽氣起於足五指之表，陰脈者，集於足下而聚於足心，故陽氣勝，則足下熱也。帝曰：寒厥之為寒也，必從五指而上於膝者，何也。歧伯曰：陰氣起於五指之裏，集於膝下而聚於膝上，故陰氣勝，則從五指至膝上寒，其寒也，不從外，皆從內也。帝曰：寒厥何失而然也。歧伯曰：前陰者，宗筋之所聚，太陰陽明之所合也。春夏則陽氣多而陰氣少，秋冬則陰氣盛而陽氣衰，此人者質壯，以秋冬奪於所用，下氣上爭不能復，精氣溢下，邪氣因從之而上也；氣因於中，陽氣衰，不能滲營其經絡，陽氣日損，陰氣獨在，故手足為之寒也。帝曰：熱厥何如而然也。歧伯曰：酒入於胃，則絡脈滿而經脈虛；脾主為胃行其津液者也，陰氣虛，則陽氣入，陽氣入，則胃不和，胃不和，則精氣竭，精氣竭，則不營其四支也。此人必數醉若飽以入房，氣聚於脾中不得散，酒氣與穀氣相薄，熱盛於中，故熱徧於身內熱而溺赤也。夫酒氣盛而慓悍，腎氣有衰，陽氣獨勝，故手足為之熱也。帝曰：厥或令人腹滿，或令人暴不知人，或至半日遠至一日乃知人者，何也。歧伯曰：陰氣盛於上則下虛，下虛則腹脹滿；陽氣盛於上，則下氣重上而邪氣逆，逆則陽氣亂，陽氣亂則不知人也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: What is meant by the cold and heat of 厥 (jue)? Qibo replied: When yang qi declines in the lower part, it becomes cold jue; when yin qi declines in the lower part, then it becomes heat jue. The Emperor asked: In the case of heat jue, when the heat arises, it must originate from beneath the feet; why is this so? Qibo said: Yang qi originates on the surface of the five toes of the foot. The yin meridians gather beneath the feet and converge at the sole of the foot; therefore, when yang qi prevails, there is heat in the lower part of the feet. The Emperor asked: In the case of cold jue, when cold occurs, it must ascend from the five toes up to the knees; why is this so? Qibo said: Yin qi originates in the inner part of the five toes, gathers below the knees and converges above the knees. Therefore, when yin qi prevails, cold arises from the five toes up to above the knees; this cold does not come from outside but originates internally. The Emperor asked: What is the cause of error that leads to cold jue? Qibo said: The anterior genital region is where the root tendons gather, and it is also where the Taiyin and Yangming meridians converge. In spring and summer, yang qi is abundant while yin qi is scarce; in autumn and winter, yin qi becomes strong while yang qi declines. In this case, the person has a robust constitution but during autumn or winter excessive use drains his resources. The descending qi struggles to ascend and cannot recover. As a result, vital essence flows downward excessively, allowing pathogenic factors to follow and move upward. The qi originates internally; yang qi declines and cannot nourish the meridians and collaterals. As yang qi diminishes daily, yin qi remains alone, hence the hands and feet become cold. The Emperor asked: What causes heat jue to occur in this way? Qibo said: When alcohol enters the stomach, the collateral vessels become full while the meridians become empty; the spleen is in charge of transporting body fluids for the stomach. When yin qi becomes deficient, yang qi enters; when yang qi enters, the stomach becomes disharmonious. If the stomach is disharmonious, vital essence and qi become exhausted; if vital essence and qi are exhausted, they cannot nourish the four limbs. This person must frequently become drunk or overeat before engaging in sexual activity, causing qi to accumulate in the spleen and not disperse. Alcohol qi combines with food qi, pressing against each other, generating intense heat internally; thus, heat spreads throughout the body, internal heat causes red-colored urine. Alcohol qi is strong and aggressive; when kidney qi declines, yang qi prevails alone, hence the hands and feet feel hot. The Emperor asked: Jue may cause someone to have abdominal distension, or suddenly lose consciousness, or remain unconscious for half a day up to an entire day before regaining awareness; why is this so? Qibo said: When yin qi becomes excessive in the upper part, the lower part becomes deficient; when the lower part is deficient, abdominal distension and fullness occur; when yang qi becomes excessive in the upper region, the descending qi rises upward and pathogenic factors move against their normal direction. When this reversal occurs, yang qi becomes disordered; when yang qi is disordered, one loses consciousness.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
相關討論
	厥論:	
帝曰：善。願聞六經脈之厥狀病能也。歧伯曰：巨陽之厥，則腫首頭重，足不能行，發為眴仆；陽明之厥，則癲疾欲走呼，腹滿不得臥面赤而熱，妄見而妄言；少陽之厥，則暴聾頰腫而熱，脇痛，䯒不可以運；太陰之厥，則腹滿䐜脹後不利不欲食，食則嘔不得臥；少陰之厥，則口乾溺赤，腹滿心痛；厥陰之厥，則少腹腫痛，腹脹涇溲不利，好臥屈膝，陰縮腫䯒內熱，盛則寫之，虛則補之，不盛不虛，以經取之。太陰厥逆，䯒急攣，心痛引腹，治主病者，少陰厥逆，虛滿嘔變，下泄清，治主病者；厥陰厥逆，攣腰痛，虛滿前閉譫言，治主病者；三陰俱逆，不得前後，使人手足寒，三日死。太陽厥逆，僵仆嘔血善衂，治主病者；少陽厥逆，機關不利，機關不利者，腰不可以行，項不可以顧，發腸癰不可治驚者死，陽明厥逆喘欬身熱，善驚衂嘔血。手太陰厥逆，虛滿而欬，善嘔沫治主病者；手心主少陰厥逆，心痛引喉身熱死不可治。手太陽厥逆，耳聾泣出，項不可以顧，腰不可以俛仰，治主病者；手陽明少陽厥逆，發喉痺，嗌腫痓，治主病者。
		The Emperor said: Well said. I would like to hear about the manifestations and symptoms of jue in the six meridians' pulses. Qibo said: When jue occurs in the Taiyang meridian, it causes swelling of the head and heaviness in the head; the feet cannot move, and symptoms such as dizziness and fainting may occur. When jue occurs in the Yangming meridian, it manifests as mania with a desire to run and shout; abdominal distension prevents lying down, the face becomes red and hot, and one experiences delusions and speaks incoherently. When jue occurs in the Shaoyang meridian, it results in sudden deafness, swelling and heat of the cheeks, pain under the ribs, and inability to move the shoulders. When jue occurs in the Taiyin meridian, it causes abdominal distension and bloating, difficulty with urination or defecation, lack of appetite; if food is consumed, vomiting occurs and lying down becomes difficult. When jue occurs in the Shaoyin meridian, it leads to dry mouth, red-colored urine, abdominal distension, and chest pain. When jue occurs in the Jueyin meridian, there is swelling and pain in the lower abdomen, abdominal distension with difficulty urinating or defecating, a preference for lying down with bent knees, retracted and swollen genitalia, internal heat. If it is excessive, drain it; if deficient, tonify it; if neither excessively strong nor weak, treat according to the meridian. When Taiyin jue becomes reversed, the genitalia become tense and spastic; chest pain radiates to the abdomen. Treat according to the main disease pattern. When Shaoyin jue becomes reversed, there is deficiency with distension, vomiting, diarrhea of clear fluids—treat according to the main disease pattern; when Jueyin jue becomes reversed, it causes spasm and lower back pain, deficiency with distension, obstruction of urination in the anterior region, and delirious speech—treat according to the primary disease pattern; when all three yin meridians are reversed, urination and defecation become impossible, causing coldness in the hands and feet; death occurs within three days. When Taiyang jue becomes reversed, it results in rigidity with fainting, vomiting blood, and frequent nosebleeds—treat according to the principal disease pattern; when Shaoyang jue becomes reversed, the joints and tendons become obstructed; when joint movement is impaired, the waist cannot support walking, the neck cannot turn. If intestinal abscess develops, it is untreatable—those who experience sudden shock will die. When Yangming jue becomes reversed, symptoms include shortness of breath, coughing, body heat, frequent startle responses, nosebleeds, and vomiting blood. When jue in the hand Taiyin meridian becomes reversed, it causes deficiency with distension and coughing, frequent vomiting of froth—treat according to the dominant disease pattern; when jue in the hand Jueyin (Heart) and Shaoyin meridians becomes reversed, chest pain radiates to the throat with body heat—this is a fatal condition that cannot be treated. When jue in the hand Taiyang meridian becomes reversed, symptoms include deafness with tears flowing, inability to turn the neck, and inability to bend or straighten the waist—treat according to the major disease pattern; when jue in the hand Yangming and Shaoyang meridians becomes reversed, it causes pharyngeal obstruction, throat swelling with spasm—treat according to the predominant disease pattern. 


46. 病能論 Discourse on Disease Manifestations

1
打開字典
	病能論:	
黃帝問曰：人病胃脘癰者，診當何如。歧伯對曰：診此者，當候胃脈，其脈當沈細，沈細者氣逆，逆者，人迎甚盛，甚盛則熱，人迎者胃脈也，逆而盛，則熱聚於胃口而不行，故胃脘為癰也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: When a person is suffering from an abscess in the stomach region, how should it be diagnosed? Qibo replied: To diagnose this condition, one should examine the stomach meridian pulse. The pulse should be deep and fine; when a pulse is deep and fine, it indicates reversed qi movement. When there is reversal, the Renying (carotid artery) becomes greatly excessive. When it is greatly excessive, heat arises. The Renying corresponds to the stomach meridian. If the condition is both reversed and excessive, then heat accumulates at the entrance of the stomach and does not disperse; thus, an abscess forms in the epigastric region.

2
打開字典
	病能論:	
帝曰：善。人有臥而有所不安者何也。歧伯曰：藏有所傷，及精有所之寄則安，故人不能懸其病也。
		The Emperor said: Well said. Why is it that some people, when lying down, feel uneasy? Qibo said: When the zang organs are injured, and if essence has a place to reside, then one feels at ease. Therefore, people cannot remain suspended in their illness.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	病能論:	
帝曰：人之不得偃臥者，何也。岐伯曰：肺者，藏之蓋也，肺氣盛則脈大，脈大則不得偃臥，論在奇恒陰陽中。
		The Emperor asked: Why is it that some people are unable to lie on their sides? Qibo said: The lungs are the canopy of the zang organs. When lung qi is excessive, the pulse becomes large; when the pulse is large, one cannot lie on the side. This theory is discussed in the section on Qi Heng Yin Yang (The Extraordinary Vessels and Yin-Yang).

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	病能論:	
帝曰：有病厥者，診右脈沈而緊，左脈浮而遲，不然病主安在。歧伯曰：冬診之右脈固當沈緊，此應四時，左脈浮而遲，此逆四時。在左當主病在腎，頗關在肺，當腰痛也。帝曰：何以言之。歧伯曰：少陰脈貫腎絡肺，今得肺脈，腎為之病，故腎為腰痛之病也。
		The Emperor asked: There is a person suffering from jue (a condition of cold extremities), and the right pulse is deep and tense, while the left pulse is floating and slow. In this case, where exactly does the illness reside? Qibo said: During winter, it is natural for the right pulse to be deep and tense; this corresponds with the four seasons. However, a floating and slow left pulse goes against the seasonal norms. The condition on the left indicates illness in the kidneys; it is also somewhat related to the lungs, and should manifest as lower back pain. The Emperor asked: On what basis do you say this? Qibo said: The Shaoyin meridian passes through the kidneys and connects to the lungs. Now, a lung pulse is observed, which indicates that the kidney has become diseased; therefore, the kidney is the source of lower back pain.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	病能論:	
帝曰：善。有病頸癰者，或石治之，或鍼灸治之，而皆已，其真安在。歧伯曰：此同名異等者也。夫癰氣之息者，宜以鍼開除去之；夫氣盛血聚者，宜石而寫之。此所謂同病異治也。
		The Emperor said: Well said. There is a case of an abscess on the neck; sometimes it is treated with stone therapy, and sometimes with acupuncture or moxibustion, and in both cases the condition resolves. Where exactly does its true nature lie? Qibo said: This is a case of the same name but different classifications. For an abscess caused by stagnant qi, it should be treated with acupuncture to open and remove the obstruction; For a condition where qi is excessive and blood accumulates, it should be drained using stone therapy. This is what is meant by treating the same disease with different methods.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	病能論:	
帝曰：有病怒狂者此病安生。歧伯曰：生於陽也，帝曰：陽何以使人狂。歧伯曰：陽氣者因暴折而難決，故善怒也，病名曰陽厥。帝曰：何以知之。歧伯曰：陽明者常動，巨陽少陽不動，不動而動大疾，此其候也。帝曰：治之柰何。歧伯曰：奪其食即已。夫食入於陰，長氣於陽，故奪其食即已。使之服以生鐵洛為飲，夫生鐵洛者，下氣疾也。
		The Emperor asked: There are those who suffer from anger and madness; where does this illness originate? Qibo said: It originates in the yang. The Emperor asked: How can yang cause a person to become mad? Qibo said: Yang qi, when suddenly obstructed and difficult to resolve, leads to frequent anger. This illness is called yang jue (yang reversal). The Emperor asked: How do you know this? Qibo said: The Yangming meridian is usually active, while the Taiyang and Shaoyang are normally inactive. When they become suddenly very active despite being normally inactive, this is its diagnostic sign. The Emperor asked: How should it be treated? Qibo said: Simply deprive the patient of food and the condition will resolve. Food enters through the yin, nourishing yang qi; therefore, depriving the patient of food resolves the condition. Have them drink a decoction made from native copper (sheng tie luo), as this substance rapidly descends qi.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	病能論:	
帝曰：善。有病身熱解墯，汗出如浴，惡風少氣，此為何病。歧伯曰：病名曰酒風。帝曰：治之奈何。歧伯曰：以澤瀉朮各十分，麋銜五分，合以三指撮為後飯。
		The Emperor said: Well said. There is a condition in which the body feels hot, with loosening of the joints, sweating as if bathing, aversion to wind, and deficiency of qi. What illness is this? Qibo said: This illness is called jiu feng (wine-induced wind). The Emperor asked: How should it be treated? Qibo said: Take ten parts each of Ze Xie (Alismatis Rhizoma) and Zhu (Atractylodis Macrocephalae Rhizoma), five parts Mi Xian (Spatholobi Caulis), combine them, and take one measure with three fingers' worth as a post-meal remedy.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	病能論:	
所謂深之細者，其中手如鍼也，摩之切之，聚者堅也；博者大也。上經者，言氣之通天也，下經者，言病之變化也，金匱者，決死生也，揆度者，切度之也，奇恒者，言奇病也。所謂奇者，使奇病不得以四時死也，恒者得以四時死也。所謂揆者，方切求之也，言切求其脈理也，度者得其病處，以四時度之也。
		What is meant by deep and fine pulses is that they feel like needles in the hand; when palpated closely, the pulse feels firm and concentrated. A broad (bo) pulse means a large one. The Shangjing discusses the communication of qi with heaven; the Xiaojing discusses changes in disease. The Jinkui determines life and death. Kuidu refers to palpation and measurement. Qi Heng refers to unusual or extraordinary diseases. What is meant by "qi" (extraordinary) is that those with such conditions do not necessarily die according to the four seasons; whereas, for those with "heng" (constant or regular), their deaths can be determined in accordance with the four seasons. What is meant by "kui" (measurement) is to carefully examine and seek it out, referring specifically to the careful examination of pulse patterns. What is meant by "du" (estimation or measurement) is identifying the location of the disease and assessing it according to the four seasons. 

47. 奇病論 Discourse on Unusual Diseases

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	奇病論:	
黃帝問曰：人有重身，九月而瘖，此為何也。歧伯對曰：胞之絡脈絕也。帝曰：何以言之。歧伯曰：胞絡者繫於腎，少陰之脈，貫腎繫舌本，故不能言。帝曰：治之奈何。歧伯曰：無治也，當十月復。刺法曰：無損不足，益有餘，以成其疹，然後調之。所謂無損不足者，身羸瘦，無用鑱石也；無益其有餘者，腹中有形而泄之，泄之則精出而病獨擅中，故曰疹成也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: When a woman is pregnant and becomes mute in the ninth month, what is this? Qibo replied: It is because the collateral vessels of the fetal sac are cut off. The Emperor asked: On what basis do you say this? Qibo said: The collateral vessels of the fetal sac connect to the kidneys. The Shaoyin meridian passes through the kidneys and connects to the base of the tongue; therefore, she cannot speak. The Emperor asked: How should this be treated? Qibo said: There is no treatment needed; it will naturally recover in the tenth month. The acupuncture method says: Do not harm what is insufficient, nor add to what is excessive; allow the eruption to form completely, and then regulate it. What is meant by "do not harm what is insufficient" is that when a person's body is emaciated and weak, one should not use acupuncture stones. What is meant by "do not add to what is excessive" is that when there is a tangible condition in the abdomen and one purges it, doing so will cause essence to be expelled while the disease alone dominates internally; therefore, this is called the eruption having formed.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	奇病論:	
帝曰：病脇下滿氣逆，二三歲不已，是為何病。歧伯曰：病名曰息積，此不妨於食，不可灸刺，積為導引服藥，藥不能獨治也。
		The Emperor asked: When a person suffers from fullness beneath the costal region and upward reversal of qi, which persists for two or three years without ceasing, what is this disease? Qibo said: The name of the disease is Xi Ji. This condition does not interfere with eating, and it cannot be treated by moxibustion or acupuncture. For accumulation-type conditions, one should employ guiding and leading exercises (qigong) along with medication; however, medicine alone cannot cure it.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	奇病論:	
帝曰：人有身體髀股䯒皆腫，環齊而痛是為何病。歧伯曰：病名曰伏梁，此風根也，其氣溢於大腸，而著於肓，肓之原在齊下，故環齊而痛也。不可動之，動之，為水溺濇之病也。
		The Emperor asked: When a person has swelling in the body, thighs, hips, and calves, with pain encircling the waist, what is this disease? Qibo said: The name of the disease is Fu Liang. This is a root condition caused by wind; its qi overflows into the large intestine and lodges in the 肓 membrane, whose origin lies below the navel; therefore, pain encircles the waist. It should not be moved or disturbed; if it is disturbed, it will cause a condition of difficulty in urination and water retention.

4
打開字典
	奇病論:	
帝曰：人有尺脈數甚，筋急而見，此為何病。歧伯曰：此所謂疹筋，是人腹必急，白色黑色見，則病甚。
		The Emperor asked: When a person has an extremely rapid cun pulse, with tense tendons that are visible, what is this disease? Qibo said: This is called "eruption of the tendons." In such a person, the abdomen must be tense. If white or black colors appear on the face, then the condition is severe.

5
打開字典
	奇病論:	
帝曰：人有病頭痛以數歲不已，此安得之，名為何病。歧伯曰：當有所犯大寒，內至骨髓，髓者以腦為主，腦逆故令頭痛，齒亦痛。病名曰厥逆。帝曰：善。
		The Emperor asked: When a person suffers from headache for several years without ceasing, how does this condition arise, and what is its name? Qibo said: This condition arises when a person is exposed to intense cold, which penetrates inward to the bone marrow. Since marrow is primarily governed by the brain, and the brain's qi becomes reversed, it causes headache as well as toothache. The name of this disease is Jue Ni. The Emperor said: Well said.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	奇病論:	
帝曰：有病口甘者，病名為何，何以得之。歧伯曰：此五氣之溢也，名曰脾癉。夫五味入口，藏於胃，脾為之行其精氣，津液在脾，故令人口甘也；此肥美之所發也，此人必數食甘美而多肥也，肥者令人內熱，甘者令人中滿，故其氣上溢，轉為消渴。治之以蘭，除陳氣也。
		The Emperor asked: When a person has a sweet taste in the mouth, what is the name of this condition, and how does it arise? Qibo said: This is an overflow of the five qi; its name is Pi Lu. The five flavors enter the mouth and are stored in the stomach; the spleen transports their refined qi. Body fluids reside in the spleen, hence causing a sweet taste in the mouth. This condition arises from excessive consumption of rich and sweet foods. Such a person must frequently consume sweet, delicious, and fatty foods; fat causes internal heat, while sweetness leads to fullness in the middle jiao (abdomen). Therefore, their qi overflows upward and transforms into xiaoke (diabetes mellitus). It should be treated with orchid herbs to eliminate stale and stagnant qi.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	奇病論:	
帝曰：有病口苦，取陽陵泉，口苦者病名為何，何以得之。歧伯曰：病名曰膽癉。夫肝者中之將也，取決於膽，咽為之使。此人者，數謀慮不決，故膽虛氣上溢，而口為之苦。治之以膽募俞，治在陰陽十二官相使中。
		The Emperor asked: When a person has bitter taste in the mouth, acupuncture at Yanglingquan is taken. What is the name of this condition and how does it arise? Qibo said: The name of this disease is Dan Lu. The liver is the general of the middle jiao, and its actions depend on the gallbladder; the throat serves as its messenger. This person frequently engages in indecisive planning and deliberation, so the gallbladder becomes deficient and qi rises upward, resulting in a bitter taste in the mouth. It should be treated by needling the front-mu and back-shu points of the gallbladder, as described in the section on the twelve internal organs and their mutual relationships.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	奇病論:	
帝曰：有癃者，一日數十溲，此不足也；身熱如炭，頸膺如格，人迎躁盛，喘息氣逆，此有餘也，太陰脈微細如髮者，此不足也。其病安在，名為何病。歧伯曰：病在太陰，其盛在胃頗在肺，病名曰厥，死不治。此所謂得五有餘二不足也。帝曰：何謂五有餘，二不足。歧伯曰：所謂五有餘者五病之氣有餘也；二不足者，亦病氣之不足也。今外得五有餘內得二不足，此其身不表不裏，亦正死明矣。
		The Emperor asked: When a person suffers from enuresis, urinating dozens of times in one day, this indicates insufficiency; łoż The body is hot as if burned by charcoal, the neck and chest feel obstructed, the Rensheng pulse is restless and strong, there is shortness of breath with upward reversal of qi—this indicates excess. However, when the Taiyin pulse is faint and thin like a hair, this indicates deficiency. Qibo said: The disease lies in the Taiyin, with excess primarily in the stomach and secondarily in the lungs. Its name is Jue, a fatal condition that cannot be treated. This is what is meant by "having five excesses but two deficiencies." The Emperor asked: What are the five excesses and the two deficiencies? Qibo said: The so-called "five excesses" refer to an excess of qi from five pathological conditions; The two deficiencies also refer to a deficiency in the disease's qi. Now, externally there is an excess of five pathological conditions and internally there is a deficiency in two aspects; this condition neither manifests on the exterior nor resides within the interior—its fatal outcome is clearly evident.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	奇病論:	
帝曰：人生而有病巔疾者，病名曰何，安所得之。歧伯曰：病名為胎病，此得之在母腹中時，其母有所大驚，氣上而不下，精氣并居，故令子發為巔疾也。
		The Emperor asked: When a person is born with an illness resembling epilepsy, what is the name of such a disease, and how does one acquire it? Qibo said: The name of this disease is Tai Bing. It originates when the mother, while carrying the child in her womb, experiences a great fright; her qi rises upward instead of descending, and refined essence and qi accumulate together, thus causing the child to develop an epilepsy-like condition.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	奇病論:	
帝曰：有病痝然如有水狀，切其脈大緊，身無痛者，形不瘦不能食，食少，名為何病。歧伯曰：病生在腎，名為腎風。腎風而不能食善驚，驚已，心氣痿者死。帝曰：善。
		The Emperor asked: When a person appears swollen as if filled with water, their pulse is large and taut, yet they experience no pain in the body; there is no emaciation, but they cannot eat well and have reduced food intake—what is this disease called? Qibo said: The disease originates in the kidneys; it is named Shen Feng. Shen Feng causes an inability to eat and a tendency toward startlement; once the startlement occurs, if the heart qi becomes atrophied, death will follow. The Emperor said: Well said. 

48. 大奇論 Treatise on Great Wonders

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	大奇論:	
肝滿腎滿肺滿皆實，即為腫。肺之雍喘而兩胠滿，肝雍兩胠滿，臥則驚，不得小便；腎雍腳下至少腹滿，脛有大小，髀䯒大跛，易偏枯。
		If the liver is full, the kidneys are full, and the lungs are full, all being solid, this is called swelling. When the lung is obstructed, it causes shortness of breath and fullness on both sides of the chest; when the liver is obstructed, there is also fullness on both sides of the body, lying down causes startlement, and urination becomes difficult; When the kidney is obstructed, fullness occurs from beneath the feet up to the lower abdomen; the calves may swell or shrink, the thighs and hips become large and lame, easily leading to hemiplegia.

2
打開字典
	大奇論:	
心脈滿大，癇瘛筋攣。肝脈小急，癇瘛筋攣。肝脈騖暴，有所驚駭，脈不至若瘖，不治自已。腎脈小急，肝脈小急，心脈小急，不鼓皆為瘕。腎肝并沈為石水，并浮為風水，并虛為死，并小絃欲驚。
		If the heart pulse is full and large, it causes epilepsy, convulsions, and muscle cramps. If the liver pulse is small and tense, it causes epilepsy, convulsion, and muscle cramp. If the liver pulse suddenly becomes rapid, there will be a startlement or fright; if the pulse does not arrive as expected or speech is lost, it will resolve on its own without treatment. If the kidney pulse is small and taut, if the liver pulse is small and taut, or if the heart pulse is small and taut—if none of them beat strongly, they are all signs of a tumor (jiasha). When the kidney and liver pulses are both deep, it is called "stone water"; when they are both floating, it is "wind water"; when they are both weak, it indicates death; and when they are small and wiry, there will be a desire to startle.

3
打開字典
	大奇論:	
腎脈大急沈，肝脈大急沈，皆為疝，心脈搏滑急為心疝。肺脈沈搏為肺疝。三陽急為瘕，三陰急為疝，二陰急為癇厥，二陽急為驚。
		lık If the kidney pulse is large, taut, and deep, or if the liver pulse is large, taut and deep, both indicate hernia; if the heart pulse is forceful, slippery, and taut, it indicates cardiac hernia. If the lung pulse is deep and forceful, it indicates pulmonary hernia. When the three Yang pulses are taut, it is a tumor (jiasha); when the three Yin pulses are taut, it indicates hernia; when the two Yin pulses are taut, epilepsy and syncope occur; when the two Yang pulses are taut, startlement occurs.

4
打開字典
	大奇論:	
脾脈外鼓，沈為腸澼，久自已，肝脈小緩為腸澼易治。腎脈小搏，沈為腸澼，下血血溫身熱者死。心肝澼亦下血，二藏同病者可治。其脈小沈濇為腸澼，其身熱者死，熱見七日死。
		If the spleen pulse beats outwardly and is deep, it indicates intestinal discharge; this condition will resolve on its own after some time. If the liver pulse is small and slow, it also indicates intestinal discharge but is easily treatable. If the kidney pulse is weak yet forceful and deep, it signifies intestinal discharge; if there is bloody stool with warm blood and fever of the body, death will occur. Heart and liver discharges also cause bloody stools; if two organs are affected simultaneously, it is treatable. If the pulse is small, deep, and rough, it indicates intestinal discharge; if the body shows fever, death will occur, and those who show fever for seven days will die.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	大奇論:	
胃脈沈鼓濇胃外鼓大，心脈小堅急，皆鬲偏枯。男子發左，女子發右，不瘖舌轉，可治，三十日起。其從者瘖，三歲起。年不滿二十者，三歲死。
		If the stomach pulse is deep, forceful, and rough; if it beats outwardly and becomes large; or if the heart pulse is weak, firm, and taut—all indicate obstruction and hemiplegia. In men, symptoms appear on the left; in women, they appear on the right. If speech is not lost and the tongue can move freely, it is treatable, with recovery expected within thirty days. Those who follow this pattern will lose their voice; recovery may take three years. Those under twenty years of age will die within three years.

6
打開字典
	大奇論:	
脈至而搏，血衂身熱者死，脈來懸鉤浮為常脈。脈至如喘名曰暴厥，暴厥者，不知與人言。脈至如數，使人暴驚，三四日自已。
		If the pulse arrives forceful and there is nosebleed with body fever, death will occur; if the pulse comes suspended, hooked, or floating, it is considered a normal pulse. If the pulse arrives like panting, it is called sudden syncope; in cases of sudden syncope, the person loses consciousness and cannot speak with others. If the pulse comes rapid, it causes a sudden startle; this condition resolves on its own within three or four days.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	大奇論:	
脈至浮合，浮合如數，一息十至以上，是經氣予不足也，微見九十日死，脈至如火薪然，是心精之予奪也，草乾而死；脈至如散葉，是肝氣予虛也，木葉落而死；脈至如省客，省客者，脈塞而鼓，是腎氣予不足也，懸去棗華而死；脈至如丸泥是胃精予不足也，榆莢落而死；脈至如橫格，是膽氣予不足也，禾熟而死；脈至如弦縷，是胞精予不足也，病善言，下霜而死，不言可治；脈至如交漆，交漆者，左右傍至也，微見三十日死；脈至如涌泉浮鼓，肌中太陽氣予不足也，少氣味，韭英而死。脈至如頹土之狀，按之不得，是肌氣予不足也，五色先見黑白壘發死；脈至如懸雍，懸雍者浮，揣切之益大，是十二俞之予不足也，水凝而死；脈至如偃刀，偃刀者，浮之小急，按之堅大急，五藏菀熟，寒熱獨并於腎也，如此其人不得坐，立春而死，脈至如丸滑不直手，不直手者，按之不可得也，是大腸氣予不足也，棗葉生而死；脈至如華者，令人善恐，不欲坐臥，行立常聽，是小腸氣予不足也，季秋而死。
		If the pulse arrives floating and combined, as if rapid, with ten or more beats in one breath, it indicates that the meridian qi has been insufficient for some time; if this weak condition is observed, death will occur within ninety days. If the pulse comes like burning firewood, it signifies the heart's essence being excessively drained; this leads to dryness and death. If the pulse arrives as scattered leaves, it indicates that the liver qi has been depleted for a long time; this is like tree leaves falling and death occurring. If the pulse arrives as a visiting guest, meaning it is obstructed yet forceful, this indicates that kidney qi has been insufficient for a long time; death occurs like when jujube flowers fall away. If the pulse arrives resembling a pill of clay, it indicates that stomach essence has been insufficient for some time; death occurs like the falling of elm pods. If the pulse arrives as if a horizontal bar, it indicates that gallbladder qi has been insufficient for some time, and death occurs like ripe grain. If the pulse arrives as thin as a string, it indicates that bladder essence has been insufficient for some time. The patient tends to speak excessively; death occurs with the first frost. If they do not speak excessively, treatment is possible. If the pulse arrives like crossing lacquer, meaning it comes from both sides, and this weak condition appears, death will occur within thirty days. If the pulse arrives like a gushing spring and is floating and forceful, it signifies that Taiyang qi in the muscles has been insufficient for some time: there will be shortness of breath and weakness; death occurs as leek flowers bloom. If the pulse arrives in a collapsed and loose manner, and cannot be felt upon pressing, it indicates that muscle qi has been insufficient for some time: if black and white colors appear first among the five facial colors, death will occur with the appearance of brick-like hair. If the pulse arrives like something hanging in the throat, which is floating and becomes even larger when pressed, it indicates that qi of the twelve 俞 (yú) points has been insufficient for some time: death occurs as water solidifies. If the pulse arrives like an inverted knife, meaning it feels slightly tense when lightly palpated and becomes firm and taut upon pressing, this indicates stagnation in all five zang organs, with cold and heat particularly concentrated in the kidneys. In such cases, the person cannot sit; death occurs at the beginning of spring. If the pulse arrives round and slippery but is not easily grasped by the hand—meaning it cannot be clearly felt—it signifies that large intestine qi has been insufficient for some time: death occurs when jujube leaves sprout. If the pulse arrives like blooming flowers, it causes a person to be easily frightened and unwilling to sit or lie down; they constantly walk or stand while listening attentively. This indicates that small intestine qi has been insufficient for some period of time: death occurs in late autumn. 

49. 脈解 Pulse Interpretation

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	脈解:	
太陽所謂腫腰脽痛者，正月太陽寅，寅，太陽也，正月陽氣出在上，而陰氣盛，陽未得自次也，故腫腰脽痛也。病偏虛為跛者，正月陽氣凍解地氣而出也，所謂偏虛者冬寒頗有不足者，故偏虛為跛也。所謂強上引背者，陽氣大上而爭故強上也。所謂耳鳴者，陽氣萬物盛上而躍，故耳鳴也。所謂甚則狂巔疾者，陽盡在上，而陰氣從下，下虛上實，故狂巔疾也。所謂浮為聾者，皆在氣也。所謂入中為瘖者，陽盛已衰，故為瘖也。內奪而厥，則為瘖俳，此腎虛也。少陰不至者，厥也。
		The so-called swelling, waist and gluteal pain caused by Taiyang is as follows: In the first month, Taiyang governs Yin. Yin means Taiyang. In the first month, yang qi emerges upward, while yin qi is strong; yang has not yet been able to take its proper place, hence there occurs swelling, waist, and gluteal pain. The condition of one-sided deficiency leading to lameness occurs because, in the first month, yang qi thaws and emerges from the earth's qi. What is referred to as "one-sided deficiency" means that there was somewhat insufficient warmth during winter; therefore, this one-sided deficiency results in lameness. What is referred to as "strong upward movement causing tension in the back" occurs because yang qi rises strongly and contends, hence resulting in a strong upward pull. What is referred to as tinnitus occurs because the yang qi and all things flourish and leap upward, hence causing ringing in the ears. What is referred to as severe mania or madness occurs because yang reaches the upper part completely, while yin follows downward; with deficiency below and excess above, hence there arises mania or insanity. What is referred to as "floating leading to deafness" occurs in all cases due to the qi. What is referred to as "entering inward causing muteness" occurs because yang, though flourishing, has already declined; hence it results in muteness. When the interior is depleted and there is syncope, it leads to muteness and staggering gait; this is due to kidney deficiency. If Shaoyin does not arrive, it indicates syncope.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	脈解:	
少陽所謂心脇痛者，言少陽盛也，盛者心之所表也。九月陽氣盡而陰氣盛，故心脇痛也，所謂不可反側者，陰氣藏物也，物藏則不動，故不可反側也。所謂甚則躍者，九月萬物盡衰，草木畢落而墮，則氣去陽而之陰，氣盛而陽之下長，故謂躍。
		What is referred to by Shaoyang as heart and hypochondriac pain means that Shaoyang is excessive; the excess condition manifests in the heart, which it governs. In the ninth month, yang qi is exhausted and yin qi becomes strong; therefore, there occurs heart and hypochondriac pain. What is referred to as "being unable to turn over or lie on one's side" means that yin qi stores things; when things are stored, they do not move, hence it is impossible to turn over or shift position. What is referred to as "severe cases causing leaping" occurs because in the ninth month all things decay completely, and grasses and trees fall off entirely; then qi departs from yang and moves toward yin. When this yin qi becomes excessive and yang declines downward, it is called "leaping."

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	脈解:	
陽明所謂洒洒振寒者，陽明者，午也，五月盛陽之陰也，陽盛而陰氣加之，故洒洒振寒也。所謂脛腫而股不收者，是五月盛陽之陰也，陽者衰於五月，而一陰氣上，與陽始爭，故脛腫而股不收也。所謂上喘而為水者，陰氣下而復上，上則邪客於藏府間，故為水也。所謂胸痛少氣者，水氣在藏府也，水者，陰氣也，陰氣在中，故胸痛少氣也。所謂甚則厥惡人與火，聞木音則惕然而驚者，陽氣與陰氣相薄，水火相惡，故惕然而驚也。所謂欲獨閉戶牖而處者，陰陽相薄也，陽盡而陰盛，故欲獨閉戶牖而居。所謂病至則欲乘高而歌，棄衣而走者，陰陽復爭，而外并於陽，故使之棄衣而走也。所謂客孫脈則頭痛鼻鼽腹腫者，陽明并於上，上者，則其孫絡太陰也，故頭痛鼻鼽腹腫也。
		What Yangming refers to as "shivering with cold and trembling" is because Yangming corresponds to Wu, which is the fifth month. In this month, it represents yin within the season of intense yang; when yang reaches its peak but yin qi adds on top, hence there occurs shivering with cold and trembling. What is referred to as "swelling of the shins and inability to flex the thighs" occurs because it represents yin within the intense yang of the fifth month. Yang declines in the fifth month, while initial yin qi rises upward and begins to contend with yang; therefore, there arises swelling of the shins and an inability to bend the thighs. What is referred to as "upper dyspnea and edema" occurs because yin qi descends but then rises again. When it ascends, pathogenic influences lodge between the zang-fu organs; hence, this results in water retention (edema). What is referred to as "chest pain and shortness of breath" occurs because water qi resides in the zang-fu organs. Water, here, refers to yin qi; when yin qi lingers internally, it causes chest pain and a deficiency of qi. What is referred to as "in severe cases, syncope with aversion to people and fire, and a sudden startle upon hearing the sound of wood" occurs because yang qi and yin qi clash; water and fire oppose each other, hence causing a sudden sense of alarm. What is referred to as "wanting to stay alone, closing doors and windows" occurs because yin and yang clash. When yang is exhausted and yin becomes excessive, one desires to remain secluded with doors and windows shut. What is referred to as "when the disease manifests, wanting to climb high and sing, or run about without clothes" occurs because yin and yang contend again, with pathogenic influences merging externally with yang; hence it causes one to discard clothing and run. What is referred to as "guesting the Sun meridians, causing headache, nasal discharge, and abdominal swelling" occurs because Yangming merges upward. The upper part refers to its collateral network of Taiyin; therefore, it causes headache, rhinorrhea, and abdominal edema.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	脈解:	
太陰所謂病脹者，太陰子也，十一月萬物氣皆藏於中，故曰病脹；所謂上走心為噫者，陰盛而上走於陽明，陽明絡屬心，故曰上走心為噫也；所謂食則嘔者，物盛滿而上溢，故嘔也；所謂得後與氣則快然如衰者，十二月陰氣下衰，而陽氣且出，故曰得後與氣則快然如衰也。
		What Taiyin refers to as "disease of distension" occurs because Taiyin corresponds to Zi, the eleventh month. In this month, the qi of all things is stored internally; hence it is called a disease of distension. What is referred to as "ascending upward to the heart and causing belching" occurs because yin becomes excessive and rises upward into Yangming. The collateral network of Yangming connects with the heart; therefore, it is said that ascending to the heart causes belching. What is referred to as "vomiting after eating" occurs because substances become overly full and overflow upward, hence vomiting results. What is referred to as "feeling relieved and as if weakened after defecation or passing gas" occurs because in the twelfth month, yin qi declines downward while yang qi begins to emerge; therefore, it is said one feels relief and a sense of weakening after defecating or releasing gas.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	脈解:	
少陰所謂腰痛者，少陰者，腎也，十月萬物陽氣皆傷，故腰痛也；所謂嘔欬上氣喘者，陰氣在下，陽氣在上，諸陽氣浮，無所依從，故嘔欬上氣喘也；所謂色色不能久立，久坐起則目䀮䀮無所見者，萬物陰陽不定未有主也。秋氣始至，微霜始下，而方殺萬物，陰陽內奪故目䀮䀮無所見也；所謂少氣善怒者，陽氣不治，陽氣不治，則陽氣不得出，肝氣當治而未得，故善怒，善怒者，名曰煎厥；所謂恐如人將捕之者，秋氣萬物未有畢去，陰氣少，陽氣入，陰陽相薄，故恐也；所謂惡聞食臭者，胃無氣，故惡聞食臭也；所謂面黑如地色者，秋氣內奪，故變於色也；所謂欬則有血者，陽脈傷也，陽氣未盛於上而脈滿，滿則欬，故血見於鼻也。
		What Shaoyin refers to as "lumbar pain" is because Shaoyin corresponds to the kidney. In the tenth month, the yang qi of all things becomes injured; therefore, lumbar pain occurs. What is referred to as "vomiting, coughing, and dyspnea with upward rushing qi" occurs because yin resides below while yang resides above. All the yang qi becomes floating and has no foundation to rely on; hence vomiting, coughing, and shortness of breath occur. What is referred to as "inability to stand for long, and upon rising after prolonged sitting, the eyes become dim with nothing in sight" occurs because at this time, yin and yang of all things are unstable and have not yet taken a dominant position. When autumn qi begins to arrive, light frost starts to fall, and it is the time when all things begin to wither; yin and yang internally contend, hence causing dim vision and an inability to see clearly. What is referred to as "deficiency of qi and a tendency toward anger" occurs because yang qi fails to function properly. When yang qi does not govern well, it cannot emerge outwardly; the liver qi should take charge but has not yet done so, hence leading to frequent anger. This condition of frequent anger is called Jianjue (Jian 厥). What is referred to as "fear, as if someone were about to capture one" occurs because in autumn, the qi of all things has not yet completely withdrawn; yin qi remains insufficient while yang qi enters inwardly. Yin and yang clash, hence causing fear. What is referred to as "aversion to the smell of food" occurs because the stomach lacks qi; therefore, one becomes averse to the odor of food. What is referred to as "a facial darkness resembling the color of earth" occurs because autumn qi internally depletes, hence causing changes in complexion. What is referred to as "coughing with blood" occurs because the yang meridians are injured. Yang qi has not yet fully flourished upward, but the vessels become full; when they overflow, coughing results, and hence blood appears from the nose.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	脈解:	
厥陰所謂㿉疝婦人少腹腫者，厥陰者，辰也，三月陽中之陰邪在中，故曰㿉疝少腹腫也。所謂腰脊痛不可以俛仰者，三月一振榮華萬物，一俛而不仰也。所謂㿉癃疝膚脹者，曰陰亦盛而脈脹不通，故曰㿉癃疝也。所謂甚則嗌乾熱中者，陰陽相薄而熱，故嗌乾也。
		What Jueyin refers to as "inguinal hernia and lower abdominal swelling in women" occurs because Jueyin corresponds to Chen, the third month. In this month, pathogenic yin resides within yang; therefore, it is called inguinal hernia with lower abdominal swelling. What is referred to as "lumbar and dorsal pain making it impossible to bend forward or backward" occurs because in the third month, all things begin a new cycle of flourishing; they bow downward but do not rise upward. What is referred to as "inguinal hernia, urinary retention, and skin swelling" occurs because yin also becomes excessive while the vessels swell and become obstructed; hence it is called inguinal or scrotal hernia. What is referred to as "in severe cases, dry throat and internal heat" occurs because yin and yang collide and generate heat; hence the throat becomes dry. 

50. 刺要論 Essentials of Acupuncture Theory

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺要論:	
黃帝問曰：願聞刺要。歧伯對曰：病有浮沈，刺有淺深，各至其理，無過其道；過之則內傷，不及則生外壅，壅則邪從之。淺深不得，反為大賊，內動五藏後生大病。故曰：病有在毫毛腠理者，有在皮膚者，有在肌肉者，有在脈者，有在筋者，有在骨者，有在髓者。是故刺毫毛腠理，無傷皮，皮傷則內動肺，肺動則秋病溫瘧，泝泝然寒慄；刺皮，無傷肉，肉傷則內動脾，脾動則七十二日四季之月，病腹脹煩，不嗜食；刺肉，無傷脈，脈傷則內動心，心動則夏病心痛；刺脈，無傷筋，筋傷則內動肝，肝動則春病熱而筋弛；刺筋無傷骨，骨傷則內動腎，腎動則冬病脹腰痛；刺骨，無傷髓，髓傷則銷鑠，胻酸，體解㑊然不去矣。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I would like to hear about the essentials of acupuncture. Qibo replied: Diseases have superficial or deep conditions, and needling has shallow or deep techniques; each should reach its proper level without exceeding the principle. If it is too excessive, it will cause internal injury; if insufficient, external obstruction will arise, and when there is obstruction, pathogenic factors will follow. If the depth of needling is incorrect, it becomes a great harm; internally disturbing the five zang organs and leading to serious illness afterward. Therefore it is said: There are diseases located in the hair and interstices of the skin, some in the skin itself, some in the muscles, some in the vessels, some in the tendons, some in the bones, and some in the marrow. Therefore, when needling the hair and interstices of skin, one must not injure the skin; if the skin is injured, it internally affects the lung, and when the lung is affected, autumn illness with feverish malaria will occur, causing chills and shivering. When needling the skin, one must not injure muscle; if muscle is injured, it internally affects the spleen, and when the spleen is affected, after 72 days in the months of each season, illness with abdominal distension and irritability will occur, along with a lack of appetite. When needling muscle, one must not injure vessels; if the vessels are injured, it internally affects the heart, and when the heart is affected, summer illness with chest pain will occur. When needling vessels, one must not injure tendons; if the tendons are injured, it internally affects the liver, and when the liver is affected, spring illness with heat and relaxed tendons will occur. When needling tendons, one must not injure bones; if the bones are injured, it internally affects kidney, and when the kidney is affected, winter illness with distension and lower back pain will occur. When needling bones, one must not injure marrow; if the marrow is injured, it will be consumed and scorched, causing leg soreness and a state of physical exhaustion that does not recover. 

51. 刺齊論 Discussion on Acupuncture Depths

1
打開字典
	刺齊論:	
黃帝問曰：願聞刺淺深之分。歧伯對曰：刺骨者無傷筋，刺筋者無傷肉，刺肉者無傷脈，刺脈者無傷皮，刺皮者無傷肉，刺肉者無傷筋，刺筋者無傷骨。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I would like to hear about the distinctions between shallow and deep acupuncture needling. Qibo replied: When needling the bones, do not injure the tendons; when needling the tendons, do not injure the muscles; when needling the muscles, do not injure the vessels; when needling the vessels, do not injure the skin; when needling the skin, do not injure the flesh; when needling the flesh, do not injure the sinews; and when needling the sinews, do not injure the bones.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺齊論:	
帝曰：余未知其所謂，願聞其解。歧伯曰：刺骨無傷筋者，鍼至筋而去，不及骨也。刺筋無傷肉者，至肉而去，不及筋也。刺肉無傷脈者，至脈而去，不及肉也。刺脈無傷皮者，至皮而去，不及脈也。所謂刺皮無傷肉者，病在皮中，鍼入皮中，無傷肉也。刺肉無傷筋者，過肉中筋也。刺筋無傷骨者，過筋中骨也。此之謂反也。
		The Emperor said: I have yet to understand what you mean; I would like to hear an explanation. Qibo said: "Needling the bones without injuring the tendons" means that the needle reaches the level of the tendons and is withdrawn, without reaching the bone. "Needling the tendons without injuring the muscles" means that the needle reaches the muscle level and is withdrawn, without penetrating to the tendons. "Needling the muscles without injuring the vessels" means that the needle reaches the vessel level and is withdrawn, without going deeper into the muscle. "Needling the vessels without injuring the skin" means that the needle reaches just beneath the skin and is withdrawn, without affecting the vessel. What is meant by "needling the skin without injuring the flesh" is that when the disease lies within the skin, the needle enters only to the level of the skin and does not injure the deeper flesh. "Needling the flesh without injuring the sinews" means that the needle passes through the flesh but avoids the tendons or sinews. "Needling the sinews without injuring the bones" means that the needle passes beyond the sinews and reaches the bone, but does not damage it. This is what is meant by "reversal." 

52. 刺禁論 Treatise on Needling Taboos

1
打開字典
	刺禁論:	
黃帝問曰：願聞禁數。歧伯對曰：藏有要害，不可不察，肝生於左，肺藏於右，心部於表，腎治於裏，脾為之使，胃為之巿。鬲肓之上，中有父母，七節之傍，中有小心，從之有福，逆之有咎。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I would like to hear about the taboos and numbers. Qibo replied: The viscera have vital points that cannot be ignored. The liver is born on the left, the lungs are stored on the right, the heart resides in the exterior, the kidneys govern the interior, and the spleen serves as their messenger, while the stomach functions like a marketplace. Above the diaphragm and the membrane, there are parents in the middle; beside the seven vertebrae, there is a small heart within. Following this principle brings blessings, while going against it causes misfortune.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺禁論:	
刺中心，一日死，其動為噫。刺中肝，五日死，其動為語。刺中腎，六日死，其動為嚏。刺中肺，三日死，其動為欬。刺中脾，十日死，其動為吞。刺中膽，一日半死，其動為嘔。
		If the heart is needled, death will occur in one day; its movement manifests as hiccups. If the liver is needled, death occurs within five days; its movement results in speech. If the kidneys are pierced, death will occur on the sixth day; its movement causes sneezing. If the lungs are needled, death occurs in three days; its movement is manifested as coughing. If the spleen is pierced, death will occur within ten days; its movement manifests as swallowing. If the gallbladder is needled, one will die in a day and a half; its movement causes vomiting.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺禁論:	
刺跗上，中大脈，血出不止，死。刺面，中溜脈，不幸為盲。刺頭中腦戶，入腦立死。刺舌下中脈太過，血出不止為瘖。刺足下布絡中脈，血不出為腫。刺郄中大脈，令人仆脫色。刺氣街中脈，血不出，為腫鼠僕。刺脊閒，中髓為傴。刺乳上，中乳房為腫根蝕。刺缺盆中內陷，氣泄令人喘欬逆。刺手魚腹內陷為腫。無刺大醉，令人氣亂。無刺大怒，令人氣逆。無刺大勞人，無刺新飽人，無刺大饑人，無刺大渴人，無刺大驚人。刺陰股中大脈，血出不止，死。刺客主人內陷中脈，為內漏為聾。刺膝髕出液為跛。刺臂太陰脈，出血多立死。刺足少陰脈，重虛出血，為舌難以言。刺膺中陷中肺，為喘逆仰息。刺肘中內陷，氣歸之，為不屈伸。刺陰股下三寸內陷，令人遺溺。刺掖下脇間內陷，令人欬。刺少腹中膀胱溺出，令人少腹滿。刺腨腸內陷為腫。刺匡上陷骨中脈，為漏為盲。刺關節中液出，不得屈伸。
		Needling above the ankle and striking a major vessel results in unceasing blood loss, which leads to death. Needling the face and hitting the liu pulse may result in blindness. If one needles the brain gate on the head and enters the brain, death occurs immediately. If a major vessel beneath the tongue is pierced too deeply during needling, continuous blood loss will result in muteness. Needling the network vessels on the sole of the foot and striking a vessel without causing bleeding results in swelling. Piercing a major vessel at the 郗 point causes one to collapse and lose color. Needling the qi street vessels without causing blood to flow results in swelling resembling that of a mouse, with fainting. If one needles between the vertebrae and strikes the marrow, it causes hunchback. Needling above the breast and striking the mammary gland results in swelling with root erosion. If one pierces inward into the clavicle region, it causes qi to escape, leading to coughing and dyspnea. Needling inward at the fish's abdomen on the hand results in swelling. Do not needle someone who is heavily drunk, as it causes disordered qi. Do not needle someone in a state of great anger, as it causes reversed qi flow. Do not needle someone who has been greatly exhausted, do not needle someone who is newly satiated, do not needle someone suffering from extreme hunger, do not needle someone intensely thirsty, and do not needle someone greatly startled. If a major vessel in the inner thigh is pierced during needling and blood does not stop flowing, it results in death. Needling inward at the guest master point and striking a vessel causes internal leakage and deafness. If fluid is drawn from the kneecap during needling, it results in lameness. Needling the Taiyin meridian on the arm and causing excessive bleeding leads to immediate death. Piercing the Shaoyin meridian of the foot, if it causes severe deficiency and blood loss, results in difficulty speaking with the tongue. Needling inward at the chest region and striking the lungs causes dyspnea and labored breathing while leaning backward. If one pierces inward at the elbow, qi will gather there, resulting in inability to bend or extend the joint. Needling inward three cun below the inner thigh causes involuntary urination. Piercing inward at the armpit and flank region causes coughing. Needling the lower abdomen and piercing the bladder to cause urination results in distension of the lower abdomen. Piercing inward into the calf muscle causes swelling. Needling a vessel within the hollow bone above the eye socket results in leakage and blindness. Piercing a joint and causing fluid to leak out leads to inability to bend or stretch. 

53. 刺志論 Lun on Needling Principles (Cizhi Lun) refers to a chapter or section discussing principles and records related to acupuncture techniques

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺志論:	
黃帝問曰：願聞虛實之要。歧伯對曰：氣實形實，氣虛形虛，此其常也，反此者病。穀盛氣盛，穀虛氣虛，此其常也，反此者病。脈實血實，脈虛血虛，此其常也，反此者病。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I would like to hear about the essentials of deficiency and excess. Qibo replied: When vital energy is abundant, the body form is solid; when vital energy is deficient, the body form is weak. This is the normal condition; if it is the opposite, then there is disease. When food essence is abundant, vital energy is strong; when food essence is deficient, vital energy is weak. This is the normal state; if it is contrary to this, then there is disease. refers to a condition where both food essence and vital energy are in abundance. When the pulse is full, the blood is abundant; when the pulse is weak, the blood is deficient. This is the normal condition, and if it is contrary to this then there is disease. refers to a state where both the pulse and the blood are in excess.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺志論:	
帝曰：如何而反。歧伯曰：氣虛身熱，此謂反也。穀入多而氣少，此謂反也。穀不入而氣多，此謂反也。脈盛血少，此謂反也。脈少血多，此謂反也。
		The Emperor asked: In what way does it become contrary? Qibo said: When vital energy is deficient but the body is hot, this is considered a reversal. When food intake is excessive yet vital energy remains insufficient, this is called a reversal. refers to the condition where there is abundant food consumption but low vital energy. When food intake is insufficient yet vital energy remains excessive, this is called a reversal. refers to the condition where food consumption is low but there is an excess of vital energy. When the pulse is strong yet the blood is deficient, this is called a reversal. refers to a condition in which the pulse appears full but there is insufficient blood. When the pulse is weak yet the blood is abundant, this is called a reversal. refers to a condition in which there is an abundance of blood but the pulse appears deficient.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺志論:	
氣盛身寒，得之傷寒。氣虛身熱，得之傷暑。穀入多而氣少者，得之有所脫血濕居下也。穀入少而氣多者，邪在胃及與肺也。脈小血多者，飲中熱也。脈大血少者，脈有風氣，水漿不入，此之謂也。
		When vital energy is excessive yet the body feels cold, this results from injury by cold pathogenic factors. lø When vital energy is deficient yet the body feels hot, it arises from injury caused by summer-heat pathogens. When food intake is abundant but vital energy remains insufficient, it results from blood loss and dampness accumulating in the lower part of the body. When food intake is low yet there is an excess of vital energy, it indicates that pathogenic factors are present in the stomach and the lungs. When the pulse is weak but the blood is abundant, it indicates internal heat caused by excessive consumption of hot or warming substances. When the pulse is large yet the blood is insufficient, it means that wind and qi are affecting the pulse, causing fluids to be unable to enter the body—this is what is meant by such a condition.

4
打開字典
	刺志論:	
夫實者，氣入也。虛者，氣出也。氣實者，熱也。氣虛者，寒也。入實者，左手開鍼空也。入虛者，左手閉鍼空也。
		Excess conditions arise when vital energy enters or accumulates excessively. Deficiency conditions occur when vital energy escapes or is lost. Excess of vital energy manifests as heat. Deficiency of vital energy manifests as cold. When treating an excess condition by draining, the left hand opens the needle channel. refers to a treatment method for excess conditions through drainage. When treating a deficiency condition by tonifying, the left hand closes the needle channel. refers to a therapeutic technique used in acupuncture for deficiency conditions through reinforcing. 

54. 鍼解 Needle Explanation

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	鍼解:	
黃帝問曰：願聞九鍼之解，虛實之道。歧伯對曰：刺虛則實之者，鍼下熱也，氣實乃熱也。滿而泄之者，鍼下寒也，氣虛乃寒也。菀陳則除之者，出惡血也。邪勝則虛之者，出鍼勿按。徐而疾則實者，徐出鍼而疾按之。疾而徐則虛者，疾出鍼而徐按之。言實與虛者，寒溫氣多少也。若無若有者，疾不可知也。察後與先者，知病先後也。為虛與實者，工勿失其法。若得若失者，離其法也。虛實之要，九鍼最妙者，為其各有所宜也。補寫之時者，與氣開闔相合也。九鍼之名，各不同形者，鍼窮其所當補寫也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I would like to hear an explanation of the Nine Needles and the principles of deficiency and excess. Qibo replied: When needling a deficient condition to tonify it, warmth is felt at the needle site; this warmth occurs when the qi becomes 充实 (substantial). When needling a full condition to drain it, coldness is felt at the needle site; such coldness occurs when the qi becomes deficient. To remove stagnation and old accumulations means to expel evil blood. When pathogenic factors are excessive, one should avoid reinforcing; after needle removal, do not press the site. The method of "slow insertion and quick pressing" to tonify involves slowly withdrawing the needle and quickly pressing the site. The method of "quick insertion and slow pressing" to drain involves quickly withdrawing the needle and slowly pressing the site. What is meant by tonifying or draining depends on the quantity and temperature of the qi—whether it is cold or warm, deficient or excessive. If something appears to be present yet not clearly so, its nature cannot be known quickly. To observe what comes after and before is to understand the sequence of the disease's development. When dealing with deficiency or excess, the practitioner must not deviate from the method. If one seems to gain and then lose, it is because they have departed from the correct method. The key to deficiency and excess lies in the Nine Needles; their effectiveness is most remarkable precisely because each has its appropriate application. The timing for tonifying or draining should correspond with the opening and closing of the qi. The Nine Needles have different names and forms, each suited to its specific application in tonifying or draining.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	鍼解:	
刺實須其虛者，留鍼陰氣隆至，乃去鍼也。刺虛須其實者，陽氣隆至，鍼下熱乃去鍼也。經氣已至，慎守勿失者，勿變更也。深淺在志者，知病之內外也。近遠如一者，深淺其候等也。如臨深淵者，不敢墯也。手如握虎者，欲其壯也。神無營於眾物者，靜志觀病人無左右視也。義無邪下者，欲端以正也。必正其神者，欲瞻病人目制其神，令氣易行也。
		When needling a condition of excess, one must wait for it to become deficient; the needle should be left in place until yin qi reaches its peak, then removed. When needling a condition of deficiency, one must await the arrival of substantial yang qi; when warmth is felt at the needle and yang reaches its peak, then remove the needle. Once the meridian qi has arrived, one must carefully maintain it without losing it; do not alter or change the method. The depth of insertion depends on intention; this is to discern whether the disease lies internally or externally. Treating near and distant points as one means that their depths are adjusted according to similar conditions. To act as if standing at the edge of a deep abyss is to be cautious and not neglect one's duty. To hold the needle as firmly as grasping a tiger means to ensure strength and stability. When one's spirit is not distracted by external things, it means maintaining a calm mind while observing the patient without looking left or right. To have no improper intentions in needling is to maintain an upright and proper approach. It is essential to stabilize one's spirit; this means looking into the patient's eyes to regulate their spirit, so that qi can flow more easily.

3
打開字典
	鍼解:	
所謂三里者，下膝三寸也。所謂跗之者，舉膝分易見也。巨虛者蹻足，䯒獨陷者。下廉者，陷下者也。
		What is referred to as "Sanli" (Three Miles) is three cun below the knee. What is called "Fu" refers to a location that becomes clearly visible when the knee is raised and separated. Juxu (Great Hollow) is located on the outer side of the lower leg; it is a point that appears as a depression when the foot is raised. Xialian refers to the depressed area at its location.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	鍼解:	
帝曰：余聞九鍼，上應天地四時陰陽，願聞其方，令可傳於後世以為常也。歧伯曰：夫一天二地三人四時五音六律七星八風九野，身形亦應之，鍼各有所宜，故曰九鍼。人皮應天，人肉應地，人脈應人，人筋應時，人聲應音，人陰陽合氣應律，人齒面目應星，人出入氣應風，人九竅三百六十五絡應野。故一鍼皮，二鍼肉，三鍼脈，四鍼筋，五鍼骨，六鍼調陰陽，七鍼益精，八鍼除風，九鍼通九竅，除三百六十五節氣，此之謂各有所主也。
		The Yellow Emperor said: I have heard that the Nine Needles correspond to Heaven, Earth, the Four Seasons, and Yin-Yang. I wish to learn their methods so they may be passed down for future generations as a constant practice. Qibo said: Heaven is one, Earth is two, Man is three, the Four Seasons are four, the Five Tones are five, the Six Laws (of pitch) are six, the Seven Stars are seven, the Eight Winds are eight, and the Nine Fields are nine. The human body also corresponds to these; each needle has its appropriate application, hence they are called the Nine Needles. Human skin corresponds to Heaven, human flesh corresponds to Earth, human vessels correspond to Man, human sinews correspond to the seasons, human voice corresponds to tones, the union of yin and yang qi in humans corresponds to the Six Laws (of pitch), human teeth, face, and eyes correspond to stars, the entry and exit of breath in humans correspond to winds, and the nine orifices and 365 collateral vessels in humans correspond to the Nine Fields. Therefore, the first needle treats the skin, the second treats flesh, the third treats vessels, the fourth treats sinews, the fifth treats bones, the sixth regulates yin and yang, the seventh nourishes essence, the eighth dispels wind, and the ninth unblocks the nine orifices, removing the 365 joints' qi. This is what is meant by each needle having its specific function.

5
打開字典
	鍼解:
