人心意應八風，人氣應天，人髮齒耳目五聲，應五音六律，人陰陽脈血氣應地，人肝目應之九，九竅三百六十五。人一以觀動靜天二以候五色七星應之以候髮毋澤五音一以候宮商角徵羽六律有餘不足應之二地一以候高下有餘九野一節俞應之以候閉節三人變一分人候齒泄多血少十分角之變五分以候緩急六分不足三分寒關節第九分四時人寒溫燥濕四時一應之以候相反一四方各作解。
		Human mind and intention correspond to the Eight Winds; human breath corresponds to Heaven. Human hair, teeth, ears, eyes, and five sounds correspond to the Five Tones and Six Laws (of pitch). The yin-yang meridians, blood, and qi in humans correspond to Earth. The liver and eyes in humans respond accordingly. There are nine orifices corresponding to 365 points. Human one observes movement and stillness; Heaven two waits for the five colors, with the Seven Stars corresponding to observe whether hair is lustrous or not. The Five Tones—one awaits the palaces, merchants, corners, harvests, and feathers—while the Six Laws respond to excess or deficiency. Two Earths await high or low conditions, excess or not; one Nine Field corresponds to each joint 俞 (yú) to observe whether joints are closed. Three people change by one part; one person observes teeth, blood loss, and blood quantity, with ten parts of corner variation, five parts for observing urgency or slowness, six parts for deficiency, three parts for cold in the joints, nine-tenths as the ninth division. The Four Seasons—human coldness, warmth, dryness, and dampness—correspond to one each; this is used to observe opposing conditions. Each of the four directions has its own explanation. 

55. 長刺節論 Chang Ci Jie Lun

1
打開字典
	長刺節論:	
刺家不診，聽病者言在頭，頭疾痛，為藏鍼之，刺至骨病已，上無傷骨肉及皮，皮者道也。
		The acupuncturist did not examine the patient, but listened to what the patient said about a headache. He inserted needles into the head where there was pain and retained them until the disease subsided. The needle reached the bone without damaging the flesh or skin; the skin is the path.

2
打開字典
	長刺節論:	
陰刺，入一傍四處。治寒熱。深專者，刺大藏，迫藏刺背，背俞也。刺之迫藏，藏會，腹中寒熱去而止，與刺之要，發鍼而淺出血。
		Yin cì, insert one needle with four punctures on the sides. It treats cold and heat symptoms. For deep-seated conditions, needles should be inserted into the major zang organs; when the condition is pressing on the zang, needle the back points, which are the shu (transport) points of the back. When needling near the zang organs, if the condition reaches the zang meeting points, stop when cold and heat sensations in the abdomen subside. This is an essential technique for acupuncture: insert the needle to cause slight bleeding by shallow puncture.

3
打開字典
	長刺節論:	
治腐腫者刺腐上，視癰小大深淺刺，刺大者多血，小者深之，必端內鍼為故止。
		To treat a decaying and swollen abscess, needle the site of decay. Examine the size and depth of the carbuncle before needling; for large ones, more blood will be drained, while small ones require deeper insertion. The needle must always be inserted straight inward until the condition stops.

4
打開字典
	長刺節論:	
病在少腹有積，刺皮𩩻以下，至少腹而止，刺俠脊兩傍四椎閒，刺兩髂髎季脇肋閒，導腹中氣熱下已。
		When the disease is located in the lower abdomen with accumulation, needle downward from the skin at the level of the hip bones until reaching the lower abdomen. Insert needles on both sides of the spine between the fourth vertebrae and also insert needles into the bilateral yue liao (iliac) points and the area between the last ribs. This will guide the heat and stagnant qi in the abdomen downward to resolve the condition.

5
打開字典
	長刺節論:	
病在少腹，腹痛不得大小便，病名曰疝，得之寒，刺少腹兩股閒，刺腰髁骨閒，刺而多之，盡炅病已。
		When the disease is in the lower abdomen, causing abdominal pain and difficulty with urination or defecation, it is called shan. This condition arises from cold exposure. Needles should be inserted between the lower abdomen and thighs, as well as between the waist and trochanteric bones; multiple insertions are required until all cold symptoms dissipate and the disease resolves.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	長刺節論:	
病在筋，筋攣節痛，不可以行，名曰筋痺，刺筋上為故，刺分肉閒，不可中骨也，病起筋炅，病已止。
		When the disease is in the sinews, causing cramping and joint pain to the point of inability to walk, it is called jin bi (sinew arthralgia). Needling should be performed on the sinews according to established principles; insert needles into the intermuscular spaces but avoid piercing the bones. The condition arises from cold in the sinews; when the heat dissipates, the disease stops.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	長刺節論:	
病在肌膚，肌膚盡痛，名曰肌痺，傷於寒濕，刺大分小分，多發鍼而深之，以熱為故，無傷筋骨，傷筋骨，癰發若變，諸分盡熱，病已止。
		When the disease is in the muscles and skin, causing generalized pain, it is called ji bi (muscle arthralgia), caused by injury from cold and dampness. Needles should be inserted into both large and small muscle spaces; multiple needles should be used with deeper insertion to dispel heat according to established principles. Avoid injuring the sinews and bones; if the sinews or bones are injured, abscesses may develop or complications arise. When all affected areas become warm, the disease will cease.

8
打開字典
	長刺節論:	
病在骨，骨重不可舉，骨髓酸痛，寒氣至，名曰骨痺，深者刺無傷脈肉為故，其道大分小分，骨熱病已止。
		When the disease is in the bones, causing heaviness to the point of being unable to lift them and acid pain in the bone marrow due to cold qi, it is called gu bi (bone arthralgia). For deep-seated conditions, needling should be performed without injuring the vessels or muscles according to established principles. The pathways include both large and small muscle spaces. When heat reaches the bones, the disease ceases.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	長刺節論:	
病在諸陽脈，且寒且熱，諸分且寒且熱，名曰狂，刺之虛脈，視分盡熱，病已止。
		When the disease is in all yang meridians, causing alternating cold and heat sensations, with both large and small muscle areas also experiencing alternating cold and heat, it is called kuang (mania). Needling should target deficient pulses; continue until all affected areas become warm. When this occurs, the disease will cease.

10
打開字典
	長刺節論:	
病初發，歲一發，不治月一發，不治，月四五發，名曰癲病，刺諸分諸脈，其無寒者以鍼調之，病止。
		When the disease first manifests, it recurs once a year; if left untreated, it recurs once a month; if still untreated, it recurs three to five times a month. This is called dianbing (epilepsy). Needles should be inserted into all muscle spaces and meridians. For cases without cold symptoms, adjust with needles until the disease ceases.

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	長刺節論:	
病風且寒且熱，炅汗出，一日數過，先刺諸分理絡脈，汗出且寒且熱，三日一刺，百日而已。
		Wind-related illness causes alternating cold and heat with hot sweating occurring multiple times a day. Initially, needle all muscle spaces and collateral vessels; continue needling once every three days when sweating persists along with alternating cold and heat. The condition will resolve within 100 days.

12
打開字典
	長刺節論:	
病大風，骨節重，鬚眉墮，名曰大風，刺肌肉為故，汗出百日，刺骨髓，汗出百日，凡二百日，鬚眉生而止鍼。
		A severe wind-related illness causes heavy joints and loss of beard and eyebrows, known as dafeng. Needling should follow established principles by targeting the muscles; continue until sweating occurs for 100 days, then needle the bone marrow; continue needling until another 100 days of sweating have passed. In total, after 200 days, when the beard and eyebrows regrow, stop needling. 


56. 皮部論 Skin Division Theory

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	皮部論:	
黃帝問曰：余聞皮有分部，脈有經紀，筋有結絡，骨有度量，其所生病各異，別其分部左右上下陰陽所在病之始終，願聞其道。歧伯對曰：欲知皮部以經脈為紀者，諸經皆然。陽明之陽，名曰害蜚，上下同法，視其部中有浮絡者，皆陽明之絡也，其色多青則痛，多黑則痺，黃赤則熱，多白則寒，五色皆見，則寒熱也，絡盛則入客於經，陽主外，陰主內。少陽之陽，名曰樞持，上下同法，視其部中有浮絡者，皆少陽之絡也，絡盛則入客於經，故在陽者主內，在陰者主出以滲於內，諸經皆然。太陽之陽，名曰關樞，上下同法，視其部中有浮絡者，皆太陽之絡也，絡盛則入客於經。少陰之陰，名曰樞儒，上下同法，視其部中有浮絡者，皆少陰之絡也，絡盛則入客於經，其入經也，從陽部注於經，其出者，從陰內注於骨。心主之陰，名曰害肩，上下同法，視其部中有浮絡者，皆心主之絡也，絡盛則入客於經。太陰之陰，名曰關蟄，上下同法，視其部中有浮絡者，皆太陰之絡也，絡盛則入客於經。凡十二經絡脈者皮之部也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I have heard that the skin has divisions and sections, the vessels have meridians and collaterals, the sinews have junctions and networks, and the bones have measurements. The diseases they produce are different; distinguishing their divisions, the left and right sides, upper and lower parts, yin and yang locations, and understanding the beginning and end of a disease—please enlighten me on this principle. Qibo replied: To understand the skin divisions by taking the meridians as a basis, all the meridians are like this. The yang of the Yangming is called Hai Fei. The upper and lower parts follow the same method. Observe within its division for any superficial collateral vessels; these are all collaterals of the Yangming. If their color is mostly blue, there will be pain; if mostly black, there will be numbness; if yellow or red, there will be heat; if mostly white, there will be cold. When all five colors appear together, it indicates alternating cold and heat. When the collaterals are excessive, they may penetrate inward to affect the meridians. Yang governs the exterior, while Yin governs the interior. The yang of the Shaoyang is called Shuchi. The upper and lower parts are treated in the same way. Observe within its division for superficial collateral vessels; these are collaterals of the Shaoyang. When the collaterals become excessive, they may enter inward to affect the meridians, so when located on the yang side it governs the interior, while when located on the yin side it governs outward movement and permeation into the interior—this is true for all meridians. The yang of the Taiyang is called Guanshu. The upper and lower parts share the same method; observe within its division for superficial collaterals, which are all collaterals of Taiyang. When these collaterals become excessive, they penetrate inward to affect the meridian. The yin of the Shaoyin is called Shuru. The upper and lower parts use the same method; observe for superficial collaterals within its division, which are all collaterals belonging to the Shaoyin. When these collaterals are excessive they penetrate inward into the meridians. When entering the meridian, they flow from the yang part into the meridian; when exiting, they move outward from the yin interior toward the bones. The yin of Xuzhu is called Haijian. The upper and lower sections follow the same method; observe superficial collaterals in its division, which are all the collaterals of Xuzhu. When these collaterals grow excessive, they penetrate inward into the meridian. The yin of Taiyin is called Guanzhe. The upper and lower follow the same method; observe any superficial collaterals in its section, which are all the collateral vessels of Taiyin. When these collateral vessels become excessive, they will penetrate inward to affect the meridian. The twelve collateral vessels are divisions of the skin.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	皮部論:	
是故百病之始生也，必先於皮毛，邪中之則腠理開，開則入客於絡脈，留而不去，傳入於經，留而不去，傳入於府，廩於腸胃。邪之始入於皮也，泝然起毫毛，開腠理。其入於絡也，則絡脈盛色變。其入客於經也，則感虛乃陷下。其留於筋骨之間，寒多則筋攣骨痛，熱多則筋弛骨消，肉爍䐃破，毛直而敗。帝曰：夫子言皮之十二部，其生病皆何如。歧伯曰：皮者，脈之部也。邪客於皮則腠理開，開則邪入客於絡脈，絡脈滿則注於經脈，經脈滿則入舍於府藏也，故皮者有分部，不與，而生大病也。帝曰：善。
		Therefore, when all kinds of diseases first arise, they always begin in the skin and hair. When pathogenic factors strike, the interstices between the muscles open; once opened, the pathogens enter into the collateral vessels. If they remain without dispersing, they transmit inward to the meridians; if still not dispersed, they further transmit inward to the fu organs, accumulating in the intestines and stomach. When pathogenic factors first enter the skin, the hair suddenly stands on end, and the interstices of the muscles open. When they enter the collaterals, then the collateral vessels become full and their color changes. When they penetrate into the meridians, they sense weakness and sink inward. When the pathogenic factors remain between the sinews and bones, if cold is predominant, it causes cramping of the sinews and pain in the bones; if heat is predominant, it leads to relaxation of the sinews, erosion of the bones, atrophy of the flesh, ulceration of the joints, and stiffened hair that becomes damaged. The Emperor asked: Master, you have spoken about the twelve divisions of the skin; what are the diseases they produce? Qibo said: The skin is the division of the vessels. When pathogenic factors reside in the skin, the interstices open; when they open, pathogens enter and reside in the collateral vessels. When the collateral vessels are full, they overflow into the meridians; when the meridians are full, the pathogens settle in the fu-organs and zang-organs. Therefore, since the skin has divisions, if these are not harmonized, serious diseases arise. The Emperor said: Well said. 

57. 經絡論 Meridians and Collaterals Treatise

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	經絡論:	
黃帝問曰：夫絡脈之見也，其五色各異，青黃赤白黑不同，其故何也。歧伯對曰：經有常色，而絡無常變也。帝曰：經之常色何如。歧伯曰：心赤，肺白，肝青，脾黃，腎黑，皆亦應其經脈之色也。帝曰：絡之陰陽，亦應其經乎。歧伯曰：陰絡之色應其經，陽絡之色變無常，隨四時而行也。寒多則凝泣，凝泣則青黑，熱多則淖澤，淖澤則黃赤，此皆常色，謂之無病。五色具見者，謂之寒熱。帝曰：善。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: When collateral vessels become visible, their five colors are different; they appear blue, yellow, red, white, and black. What is the reason for this? Qibo replied: The meridians have constant colors, while the collateral vessels undergo no fixed changes. The Emperor asked: What are the constant colors of the meridians? Qibo said: The heart is red, the lungs are white, the liver is blue, the spleen is yellow, and the kidneys are black; these all correspond to the colors of their respective meridians. The Emperor asked: Do the yin and yang properties of collateral vessels also correspond to those of their associated meridians? Qibo said: The colors of yin collaterals correspond to their associated meridians, while the colors of yang collaterals change without fixed pattern and vary according to the four seasons. Excessive cold causes stagnation, and stagnation results in a blue-black color; excessive heat causes moistness and luster, and moistness and luster result in a yellow-red color. These are all normal colors, indicating the absence of disease. When all five colors appear together, it is called cold-heat (a condition involving both cold and heat). The Emperor said: Well said. 

58. 氣穴論 Qi Xue Lun

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	氣穴論:	
黃帝問曰：余聞氣穴三百六十五，以應一歲，未知其所，願卒聞之。歧伯稽首再拜對曰：窘乎哉問也。其非聖帝，孰能窮其道焉，因請溢意盡言其處。帝捧手逡巡而卻曰：夫子之開余道也，目未見其處，耳未聞其數，而目以明，耳以聰矣。歧伯曰：此所謂聖人易語，良馬易御也。帝曰：余非聖人之易語也，世言真數開人意，今余所訪問者真數，發蒙解惑，未足以論也。然余願聞夫子溢志盡言其處，令解其意，請藏之金匱，不敢復出。歧伯再拜而起曰：臣請言之，背與心相控而痛，所治天突與十椎及上紀，上紀者，胃脘也，下紀者，關元也。背胸邪繫陰陽左右。如此其病前後痛濇，胸脇痛而不得息不得臥，上氣短氣偏痛，脈滿起斜出尻脈，絡胸脇支心貫鬲，上肩加天突，斜下肩交十椎下。藏俞五十穴，府俞七十二穴，熱俞五十九穴，水俞五十七穴，頭上五行行五，五五二十五穴，中𦛗兩傍各五，凡十穴，大椎上兩傍各一，凡二穴，目瞳子浮白二穴，兩脾厭分中二穴，犢鼻二穴，耳中多所聞二穴，眉本二穴，完骨二穴，頂中央一穴，枕骨二穴，上關二穴，大迎二穴，下關二穴，天柱二穴，巨虛上下廉四穴，曲牙二穴，天突一穴，天府二穴，天牖二穴，扶突二穴，天䆫二穴，肩解二穴，關元一穴，委陽二穴，肩貞二穴，瘖門一穴，齊一穴，胸俞十二穴，背俞二穴，膺俞十二穴，分肉二穴，踝上橫二穴，陰陽蹻四穴，水俞在諸分，熱俞在氣穴，寒熱俞在兩骸厭中二穴，大禁二十五，在天府下五寸，凡三百六十五穴，鍼之所由行也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I have heard that there are 365 qi xue (acupuncture points), corresponding to the days of one year, but I do not know their locations. I wish to hear about them completely. Qibo bowed and kowtowed twice in reply: What a profound question this is! If not for the Sage Emperor, who could possibly fathom its principles? Therefore, I shall speak at length and fully explain their locations. The Emperor raised his hands, hesitated for a moment, and stepped back, saying: "Master, your instruction has opened my understanding. Though I have not yet seen these locations with my eyes or heard their numbers with my ears, my vision is already clear, and my hearing is already sharp." Qibo said: This is what is meant by the saying that sages are easy to teach, and good horses are easy to ride. The Emperor said: I am not a sage who is easy to teach. The world says that true numbers can open one's mind. Now what I inquire about are these true numbers, which enlighten and resolve doubts; they are not yet sufficient for discussion. Nevertheless, I wish to hear you speak fully and completely about their locations, so that I may understand their meanings. Please allow me to preserve this knowledge in a golden chest; I shall never reveal it again. Qibo kowtowed twice and said: I shall speak. Pain that affects the back and heart is treated at Tianju, the tenth vertebra, and Shangji; Shangji refers to Weiwuan (the epigastrium), while Xijji refers to Guanyuan. The back and chest are laterally connected to the yin and yang meridians on both sides. If so, the disease will manifest as pain and obstruction in front and back, chest and hypochondriac pain with difficulty breathing and inability to lie down, upward rushing qi, shortness of breath, lateral pain, full pulse arising obliquely from the gluteal region. The collateral channels run through the thorax and hypochondrium, branch out to the heart, pass through the diaphragm, ascend to the shoulder, add Tianju (the suprasternal notch), then descend diagonally across the shoulder to below the tenth vertebra. There are fifty Zang 俞 (Zang 俞) points, seventy-two Fu 俞 (Fu 俞) points, fifty-nine Re 俞 (Re 俞) points, and fifty-seven Shui 俞 (Shui 俞) points. On the head, there are five rows with five in each row, totaling twenty-five points; on both sides of the middle part of the neck, there are five on each side, making ten points in total; above the Dazhui point, one on each side, totaling two points; Pupillary White (Puping) two points; between the spleen depressions, two points; Dubi two points; in the ear where much is heard, two points; at the base of the eyebrows, two points; Wangu two points; central vertex, one point; Zhengu two points; Shangguan two points; Daying two points; Xiaguan two points; Tianzhu two points; Juxia Shanglian and Xiaolian four points; Quya two points; Tiantu one point; Tianfu two points; Tianyou two points; Futu two points; Tianyu two points; Jianjie two points; Guanyuan one point; Weiyang two points; Jianzhen two points; Yinmen one point; Qi (the navel) one point. There are twelve Xiong 俞 (Xiong 俞) points on the chest, two Bei 俞 (Bei 俞) points on the back, twelve Ying 俞 (Ying 俞) points in the chest area, and two Fenrou points. Above the ankle, there is a horizontal line with two points; four Yin-Yang Qiao points; Shui 俞 are located at various divisions; Re 俞 are found at Qi xue (Qi points); Hanre 俞 are between the two Haiyan depressions, totaling two points. There are twenty-five Da Jin points below Tianfu by five cun. In total, there are 365 points, through which acupuncture is practiced.

2
打開字典
	氣穴論:	
帝曰：余已知氣穴之處，遊鍼之居，願聞孫絡谿谷，亦有所應乎。歧伯曰：孫絡三百六十五穴會，亦以應一歲，以溢奇邪，以通榮衛，榮衛稽留，衛散榮溢，氣竭血著，外為發熱，內為少氣，疾寫無怠，以通榮衛，見而寫之，無問所會。帝曰：善。
		The Emperor said: I have now learned the locations of the qi xue and where the needles should be inserted. I would like to hear whether the collateral channels (Sun Luo) and valleys (Xi Gu) also correspond to something. Qibo said: The Sun Luo collateral channels converge at 365 xue hui (points of convergence), which also correspond to the days in one year. They serve to expel abnormal pathogenic influences and to facilitate the flow of Ying and Wei qi. When Ying and Wei become stagnant, Wei disperses while Ying overflows; this leads to exhaustion of Qi and stagnation of blood. Externally, it manifests as fever; internally, as deficiency of Qi. Therefore, one must quickly drain without delay in order to restore the smooth flow of Ying and Wei. If such conditions are observed, they should be drained immediately, regardless of which points they converge at. The Emperor said: Well said.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	氣穴論:	
願聞谿谷之會也。歧伯曰：肉之大會為谷，肉之小會為谿，肉分之閒，谿谷之會，以行榮衛，以會大氣。邪溢氣壅，脈熱肉敗榮衛不行，必將為膿內銷骨髓，外破大膕，留於節湊，必將為敗。積寒留舍，榮衛不居，卷肉縮筋，肋肘不得伸，內為骨痺，外為不仁，命曰不足，大寒留於谿谷也。谿谷三百六十五穴會，亦應一歲。其小痺淫溢，循脈往來，微鍼所及，與法相同。
		I would like to hear about the convergence of the Xi Gu (valleys). Qibo said: The large convergence of flesh is called Gu, while the small convergence is called Xi. Between the divisions of flesh are the convergences of Xi and Gu, through which Ying and Wei circulate, and where major Qi gathers. When pathogenic influences overflow and Qi becomes obstructed, the vessels become hot, flesh decays, and Ying and Wei cease to circulate. This will inevitably lead to abscess formation, which internally consumes bone marrow and externally ruptures large joints. If stagnation occurs at the joints, it will certainly result in deterioration. Accumulated cold remaining in the body causes Ying and Wei to stagnate, leading to contracted flesh and tightened tendons. The ribs and elbows cannot extend; internally this results in bone bi (arthralgia), externally it leads to numbness and insensitivity. This is called "insufficiency," caused by severe cold lodging in the Xi Gu (valleys and streams). The 365 Xue Hui (points of convergence) of the Xi Gu also correspond to one year. When minor bi syndrome spreads and follows the vessels back and forth, it can be treated with fine needles in accordance with established methods.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	氣穴論:	
帝乃辟左右而起再拜曰：今日發蒙解惑，藏之金匱，不敢復出，乃藏之金蘭之室，署曰氣穴所在。歧伯曰：孫絡之脈別經者，其血盛而當寫者，亦三百六十五脈，並注於絡，傳注十二絡脈，非獨十四絡脈也，內解寫於中者十脈。
		The Emperor then dismissed those around him, rose and kowtowed twice, saying: "Today you have enlightened my ignorance and resolved my doubts. I shall preserve this knowledge in a golden chest and never reveal it again." Thus he stored it in the Golden Orchid Hall (Jinlan Zhishi), labeling it as "The Locations of Qi Xue." Qibo said: The Sun Luo vessels that branch off from the main meridians, when their blood is excessive and requires drainage, also number 365. These all converge into the collateral channels, transmitting to twelve major collateral vessels; it is not only the fourteen collateral vessels. Ten of these internally resolve and drain within the body. 

59. 氣府論 Treatise on the Acupoints of Qi

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	氣府論:	
足太陽脈氣所發者七十八穴；兩眉頭各一，入髮至項三寸半，傍五相去三寸，其浮氣在皮中者凡五行，行五，五五二十五，項中大筋兩傍各一，風府兩傍各一，俠背以下至尻尾二十一節十五閒各一，五藏之俞各五，六府之俞各六，委中以下至足小指傍各六俞。
		There are seventy-eight acupoints from which the qi of the Taiyang meridian of the foot originates. There is one acupoint on each of the two eyebrows; from where it enters the hairline to the back of the neck, three and a half cun in length, there are five points arranged laterally at intervals of three cun. The superficial qi flows within the skin in five rows, with five points per row—five times five makes twenty-five. There is one point on each side beside the large tendon in the nape; one point on each side adjacent to Fengfu (GV16). From below the back down to the coccyx, there are twenty-one vertebrae and fifteen spaces between them, with one acupoint at each space. For the five zang organs, there are five 俞 points for each organ; for the six fu organs, there are six 俞 points for each. Below Weizhong (BL40) down to beside the little toe of the foot, there are six 俞 points on each side.

2

		There are sixty-two acupoints from which the qi originates in the Shaoyang meridian of the foot. There is one point above and one below each anterior corner of the ear, one point beneath the sharp part of the hairline, one at Kezhuren (ST8) on each side, one in the hollow behind each ear, one at Xiaguan (ST7) on each side; one behind the jawbone under each ear. From below the knee down to the second toe of the foot, there are Six 俞 points on each side.

60. 骨空論 Bone Cavity Treatise

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	骨空論:	
黃帝問曰：余聞風者百病之始也，以鍼治之，柰何。歧伯對曰：風從外入，令人振寒，汗出頭痛，身重惡寒，治在風府，調其陰陽，不足則補，有餘則寫。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I have heard that wind is the beginning of all diseases; how should it be treated with acupuncture needles? Qibo replied: Wind that enters from the outside causes chills, perspiration, headache, body heaviness, and aversion to cold; treatment should focus on Fengfu (GV16), regulating yin and yang. If there is deficiency, tonify it; if there is excess, drain it.

2
打開字典
	骨空論:	
大風頸項痛，刺風府，風府在上椎。大風汗出，灸譩譆，譩譆在背下俠脊傍三寸所，厭之令病者呼譩譆，譩譆應手。從風憎風，刺眉頭。失枕在肩上棋骨閒，折使榆臂齊肘正，灸脊中。䏚絡季脇引少腹而痛脹，刺譩譆。腰痛不可以轉搖，急引陰鯤刺八髎與痛上，八髎在腰尻分閒。
		Severe wind causing neck and nape pain should be treated by needling Fengfu (GV16). Fengfu is located at the upper vertebra. Severe wind with perspiration should be treated by moxibustion at Yintang (GV29). Yintang is located three cun lateral to the spine, below the back. Press it and have the patient say "Yintang," and Yintang will respond accordingly. For aversion to wind due to wind invasion (aversion to drafts), needle the area between the eyebrows. Shizhen is located in the space between the shoulder and clavicle; have the patient bend their arm so that the elbow aligns straight, then moxibustion should be applied at Zhongshu (DU12) on the spine. If there is pain and distension in the lower abdomen caused by a network of meridians extending from the flank, needle Yintang. Lower back pain that prevents turning or moving, with severe pulling in the genital area—needle Baoliao and the painful point. The eight Baoliao points are located between the waist and buttocks.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	骨空論:	
鼠瘻寒熱，還刺寒府，寒府在附膝外解營。取膝上外者使之拜，取足心者使之跪。任脈者，起於中極之下，以上毛際循腹裏上關元至咽喉，上頤循面入目。衝脈者，起於氣街，並少陰之經，俠齊上行，至胸中而散。任脈為病，男子內結七疝，女子帶下瘕聚。衝脈為病，逆氣裏急。督脈為病，脊強反折。督脈者，起於少腹以下骨中央，女子入繫廷孔，其孔，溺孔之端也，其絡循陰器合篡閒繞篡後，別繞臀至少陰，與巨陽中絡者合，少陰上股內後廉，貫脊屬腎，與太陽起於目內眥，上額交巔，上入絡腦，還出別下項循肩髆，內俠脊抵腰中入循膂絡腎。其男子循莖下至篡，與女子等。其少腹直上者貫齊中央，上貫心入喉，上頤環脣，上繫兩目之下中央，此生病從少腹上衝心，而痛不得前後，為衝疝，其女子不孕，癃痔遺溺嗌乾。督脈生病治督脈，治在骨上，甚者在齊下營，其上氣有音者，治其喉，中央在缺盆中者，其病上衝喉者治其漸，漸者上俠頤也。
		For shulou with chills and fever, needle Hanfu (ST38). Hanfu is located on the outer side of the knee. To treat points above the knee on the lateral side, have the patient bow; to treat points at the sole of the foot, have them kneel. The Ren Meridian originates below Zhongji (CV3), ascends through the pubic region, follows the inner abdomen upward to Guanyuan (CV4), reaches the throat, goes up to the jaw, and proceeds along the face into the eyes. The Chong Meridian originates at Qijie (ST32), runs parallel to the Kidney Meridian, ascends on both sides of the umbilicus, and disperses in the chest. Diseases arising from the Ren Meridian manifest as seven types of hernia internally in men, and leukorrhea with abdominal masses in women. Diseases arising from the Chong Meridian present as upward-reversing qi and internal tightness. Diseases arising from the Du Meridian manifest as rigidity of the spine and severe bending backward (opisthotonus). The Du Meridian originates from the central part of the lower abdomen below the pubic bone; in women, it enters through the hymenal ring. This opening is at the end of the urinary orifice. Its collateral follows the genital organs and converges between the anus, encircling behind the perineum, then branches around the buttocks to reach the Kidney Meridian (Taiyang). It combines with the collateral of the Taiyang meridians that run centrally. The Kidney Meridian ascends along the inner posterior aspect of the thigh, passes through the spine and connects to the kidney. Together with the Taiyang, it originates at the medial canthus of the eye, ascends over the forehead to meet on the vertex, enters the brain network, then turns back down the neck, follows the shoulders and scapulae, runs inward along both sides of the spine, reaches the waist center, and proceeds downward through the erector spinae muscles into the kidney network. In men, it follows the penis down to the perineum; this is similar in women. The branch of the Du Meridian that ascends directly from the lower abdomen passes through the center of the umbilicus, upward through the heart into the throat, up to the jaw and around the lips, then connects centrally beneath both eyes. Diseases arising from this meridian manifest as pain ascending from the lower abdomen toward the heart, with obstruction preventing urination or defecation (retention), known as chong shen (upward rushing hernia). In women, it may cause infertility, urinary retention, hemorrhoids, incontinence, and dry throat. For diseases of the Du Meridian, treat along the Du Meridian. Treat points on the bones; in severe cases, treat below the umbilicus. For those with upward-reversing qi accompanied by sound, treat the throat. If the disease is centralized at the hollow between the clavicles (Quepen), and if it surges up to the throat, treat Jian (the area above the jaw). "Jian" refers to the region just above the jawline on both sides.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	骨空論:	
蹇膝伸不屈治其楗，坐而膝痛治其機。立而暑解，治其骸關。膝痛，痛及拇指治其膕。坐而膝痛如物隱者治其關。膝痛不可屈伸，治其背內。連䯒若折，治陽明中俞髎。若別治巨陽少陰滎。淫濼脛痠，不能久立，治少陽之維，在外上五寸。輔骨上橫骨下為楗，俠髖為機，膝解為骸關，俠膝之骨為連骸，骸下為輔，輔上為膕，膕上為關，頭橫骨為枕。
		For knee stiffness with inability to bend, treat Jian (a point related to the knee). For pain in the knee while sitting, treat Ji (the popliteal region behind the knee). For stiffness when standing that causes discomfort or inability to move freely, treat Haiguan (a joint-related point). Knee pain extending to the big toe should be treated at Weiyang (the popliteal fossa area). For knee pain while sitting that feels as if something is obstructing it, treat the joints. Knee pain preventing flexion or extension should be treated on the inner back (along the Bladder Meridian). If there is a sensation of tightness and bending as if broken, treat Zhongyushao (ST29) on the Yangming Stomach Meridian. Otherwise, treat Xinggu points of the Taiyang and Shaoyin meridians separately. For leg weakness with aching shins that prevents standing for long, treat the Weiyang points of the Shaoyang Meridian located five cun above on the outer side. The area above the fibula and below the transverse bone is called Jian. The region on both sides of the hip is Ji. The knee joint is Haiguan. The bones flanking the knee are Lianhai. Below Haiguan is Fu, above it is Wei (popliteal fossa), above Wei is Guan, and the transverse bone at the head is Zhen.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	骨空論:	
水俞五十七穴者，尻上五行，行五，伏菟上兩行，行五，左右各一行，行五，踝上各一行，行六穴。
		The fifty-seven Shuiyu points are arranged in five rows above the coccyx, with five points per row; two rows on the upper thigh (Futu), each containing five points; one row on either side, also with five points each; and one row above each malleolus, containing six points.

6
打開字典
	骨空論:	
髓空在腦後三分，在顱際銳骨之下，一在齗基下，一在項後中復骨下，一在脊骨上空在風府上。脊骨下空在尻骨下空。數髓空在面俠鼻，或骨空在口下當兩肩。兩髆骨空在髆中之陽。臂骨空在臂陽，去踝四寸兩骨空之間。股骨上空在股陽出上膝四寸。䯒骨空在輔骨之上端。股際骨空在毛中動下。尻骨空在髀骨之後相去四寸。肩骨有滲理湊，無髓孔，易髓無空。
		The marrow cavities are located three cun behind the occiput, beneath the sharp bone at the edge of the skull. One is below the mandibular base; one lies under the central fugu bone at the back of the neck; and another is in the hollow above the spine, just above Fengfu (GV16). The marrow cavity below the spinal column is located beneath the coccyx. Numerous marrow cavities are on both sides of the nose on the face; some bone cavities are located below the mouth, corresponding to the two shoulders. The marrow cavities in the two scapula bones are located at Yang points within the shoulder blades. The marrow cavity of the arm bone is on its yang (lateral) side, four cun above the malleolus between two bones. The marrow cavity in the upper part of the femur is located on its yang (outer) side, four cun superior to the knee. The marrow cavity of the tibia bone is at the upper end of the fibula. The marrow cavity between the thighs is located in the pubic area, below the moving part (inguinal region). The marrow cavity of the coccyx bone is four cun posterior to the femur bone. The shoulder bones have interwoven textures and seams, but no marrow cavities; their marrow is easily distributed without specific openings.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	骨空論:	
灸寒熱之法，先灸項大椎，以年為壯數，次灸橛骨，以年為壯數，視背俞陷者灸之，舉臂肩上陷者灸之，兩季脇之間灸之，外踝上絕骨之端灸之，足小指次指間灸之，腨下陷脈灸之，外踝後灸之，缺盆骨上切之堅痛如筋者灸之，膺中陷骨間灸之，掌束骨下灸之，齊下關元三寸灸之，毛際動脈灸之，膝下三寸分閒灸之，足陽明跗上動脈灸之，巔上一灸之，犬所囓之處灸之三壯，即以犬傷病法灸之，凡當灸二十九處。傷食灸之，不已者，必視其經之過於陽者，數刺其俞而藥之。
		The method for moxibustion in treating chills and fever is as follows: first, moxibust the Dazhui (GV14) at the base of the neck. The number of moxa cones should correspond to the patient's age. Next, moxibust Juegu (the spinous process), also using the patient's age for the number of cones. Moxibust any depressed points on the back 俞 points; if there are depressions at the shoulder when raising the arm, moxibust them. Moxibust between the two flanks, at the end of the "juegu" bone above the outer malleolus (Yanglingquan area), and between the little toe and fourth toe. Moxibust depressed pulses below the calf, behind the outer ankle, and where pressing on the Qupen bone causes firm pain like a tendon—moxibust there. Also moxibust in the depression between the sternum bones (Yingzhong), under the wrist bones of the palm, three cun below Guanyuan at the lower abdomen, at the pulsation at the pubic arch, and between points three cun below the knee. Moxibust the pulsating point on the Yangming Meridian above the foot's dorsum (Fenglong area). Apply one cone to the vertex of the head. For any place bitten by a dog, apply three cones; treat it according to the method for treating dog bite injuries. In total, moxibust at twenty-nine locations. For food stagnation treated with moxibustion, if symptoms persist without improvement, examine the meridians that are overly yang in nature and repeatedly needle their 俞 points while administering herbal medicine. 

61. 水熱穴論 Treatise on Acupoints for Water and Heat Disorders

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	水熱穴論:	
黃帝問曰：少陰何以主腎，腎何以主水。歧伯對曰：腎者，至陰也，至陰者，盛水也。肺者，太陰也，少陰者，冬脈也，故其本在腎，其末在肺，皆積水也。帝曰：腎何以能聚水而生病。歧伯曰：腎者，胃之關也，關門不利，故聚水而從其類也，上下溢於皮膚，故為胕腫，胕腫者，聚水而生病也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: Why is Shaoyin associated with the kidney, and why is the kidney associated with water? Qibo replied: The kidney is the utmost Yin. That which is the utmost Yin contains much water. The lung is the Great Yin, and Shaoyin is the winter pulse. Therefore, its root lies in the kidney, while its extremity lies in the lung; both accumulate water. The Emperor asked: Why can the kidney gather water and thus give rise to disease? Qibo said: The kidney is the gate of the stomach. If this gate does not function properly, water will gather and follow its nature; it overflows upward and downward into the skin, thus causing edema. Edema arises from the gathering of water and hence causes disease.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	水熱穴論:	
帝曰：諸水皆生於腎乎。歧伯曰：腎者，牝藏也，地氣上者屬於腎，而生水液也，故曰至陰。勇而勞甚則腎汗出，腎汗出逢於風，內不得入於藏府，外不得越於皮膚，客於玄府，行於皮裏，傳為胕腫，本之於腎，名曰風水。所謂玄府者，汗空也。
		The Emperor asked: Do all kinds of water-related conditions originate in the kidney? Qibo said: The kidney is a yin organ. The earth qi that rises belongs to the kidney and thus generates water and body fluids; therefore, it is called the utmost Yin. Excessive exertion by the bold and vigorous causes kidney sweat to emerge. When kidney sweat meets wind, internally it cannot enter the zang-fu organs, externally it cannot escape through the skin; instead, it lodges in the xuanfu, flows beneath the skin, and transforms into edema. Its origin lies in the kidney; this condition is called fengshui (wind-water). What is referred to as xuanfu are the pores through which sweat escapes.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	水熱穴論:	
帝曰：水俞五十七處者，是何主也。歧伯曰：腎俞五十七穴，積陰之所聚也，水所從出入也。尻上五行行五者，此腎俞。故水病，下為胕腫大腹，上為喘呼，不得臥者，標本俱病，故肺為喘呼，腎為水腫，肺為逆不得臥，分為相輸俱受者，水氣之所留也。伏菟上各二行行五者，此腎之街也。三陰之所交結於腳也。踝上各一行行六者，此腎脈之下行也，名曰太衝。凡五十七穴者，皆藏之陰絡，水之所客也。
		The Emperor asked: The fifty-seven acupoints associated with water, what do they primarily govern? Qibo said: The fifty-seven shu points of the kidney are where accumulated Yin gathers; these are the sites through which water enters and exits. Five rows with five in each row on the upper part of the coccyx—these are the kidney shu points. Therefore, in water-related diseases, downward manifestation is edema and abdominal distension; upward it causes shortness of breath. When one cannot lie down, both the root and branch are affected. Hence, the lung causes shortness of breath, while the kidney causes swelling; the lung's dysfunction prevents lying down. These conditions, which affect both upper and lower parts, result from water qi stagnation. Two rows with five points in each row above the futu—these are the channels of the kidney. They are where the three Yin meridians converge and connect at the feet. One row with six points above each ankle—this is the downward course of the kidney meridian, named Taichong. All fifty-seven points are yin collaterals of the zang organs; they are where water qi lodges.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	水熱穴論:	
帝曰：春取絡脈分肉，何也。歧伯曰：春者，木始治，肝氣始生，肝氣急，其風疾，經脈常深，其氣少，不能深入，故取絡脈分肉間。帝曰：夏取盛經分腠，何也。歧伯曰：夏者，火始治，心氣始長，脈瘦氣弱，陽氣留溢，熱熏分腠，內至於經，故取盛經分腠絕膚而病去者，邪居淺也，所謂盛經者，陽脈也。帝曰：秋取經俞，何也。歧伯曰：秋者，金始治，肺將收殺，金將勝火，陽氣在合，陰氣初勝，濕氣及體，陰氣未盛，未能深入，故取俞以寫陰邪，取合以虛陽邪，陽氣始衰，故取於合。帝曰：冬取井榮，何也。歧伯曰：冬者，水始治，腎方閉，陽氣衰少，陰氣堅盛，巨陽伏沈，陽脈乃去，故取井以下陰逆，取榮以實陽氣。故曰：冬取井榮，春不鼽衄，此之謂也。
		The Emperor asked: In spring, one should take the collateral vessels and fenshu meat—why is this so? Qibo said: Spring is the season when wood begins to govern, and liver qi begins to generate. Liver qi is quick-tempered, and its wind moves swiftly; the meridians are deep, but the qi is weak and cannot penetrate deeply, so one should take points in the collateral vessels and between fenshu muscles. The Emperor asked: In summer, one takes the full meridians and cun tissues—why is this so? Qibo said: Summer is the season of yang flourishing; the qi of the three yin meridians has already been exhausted, while the qi of the three yang meridians is abundant. Therefore, one should take points on the full meridians among the cun tissues. Qibo said: Summer is the season when fire begins to govern, and heart qi starts to grow. The pulse becomes thin and the qi weak; yang qi lingers and overflows, heat 熏 (xun) affects the cun tissues, and internally reaches the meridians. Therefore, taking points on the full meridian among the cun tissue can remove disease by penetrating the skin—this is because pathogenic factors reside shallowly. What is referred to as a "full meridian" is a yang vessel. The Emperor asked: In autumn, one takes the jing shu points—why is this so? Qibo said: Autumn is the season when metal begins to govern; lung qi starts to flourish. The skin and hair become firm, and the pores close. Pathogenic factors are deeply embedded in the meridians, so it is necessary to take the jing shu (meridian shu) points for treatment. Qibo said: Autumn is the season when Metal begins to govern; the lung will begin to gather and kill. Metal will overcome Fire, yang qi resides in the he (confluent) points, while yin qi initially prevails. Dampness affects the body, but yin qi has not yet reached full strength and cannot deeply penetrate. Therefore, shu points are taken to expel yin pathogenic factors, and he points are used to reduce yang pathogens. Since yang qi begins to decline, treatment is applied at the he points. The Emperor asked: In winter, one takes jing and rong points—why is this so? Qibo said: Winter is the season when water begins to govern; kidney qi starts to flourish. Yin reaches its peak while yang retreats inwardly. The body's pores close tightly, and pathogenic factors remain superficially on the skin surface without deeply invading. Therefore, jing (well) points are used for dispersing stagnation at the very beginning of meridians, and rong (stream) points help to regulate qi flow in shallow channels. This approach aligns with winter's nature where treatment focuses on external rather than internal conditions. Qibo said: Winter is the season when Water begins to govern; at this time, kidney function becomes closed. Yang qi declines and weakens, while yin qi grows strong and solid. The great yang energy lies dormant beneath the surface, and yang meridians recede. Therefore, jing points are taken to descend excessive yin and reverse its dominance, and rong points are used to strengthen yang qi. Therefore it is said: In winter one takes the jing and rong points; thus, in spring there will be no epistaxis or nasal discharge. This is what that means.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	水熱穴論:	
帝曰：夫子言治熱病五十九俞，余論其意，未能領別其處，願聞其處，因聞其意。歧伯曰：頭上五行行五者，以越諸陽之熱逆也。大杼膺俞缺盆背俞此八者，以寫胸中之熱也。氣街三里巨虛上下廉此八者，以寫胃中之熱也。雲門髃骨委中髓空此八者，以寫四支之熱也。五藏俞傍五此十者，以寫五藏之熱也。凡此五十九穴者，皆熱之左右也。帝曰：人傷於寒而傳為熱，何也。歧伯曰：夫寒盛，則生熱也。
		The Emperor said: You have spoken of the fifty-nine shu points for treating febrile diseases. I have considered your meaning, but still cannot clearly distinguish their locations. I wish to hear about these locations and thus understand their significance. Qibo said: Five rows with five points each on the head—these are used to relieve heat reversal in all yang meridians. Dazhu, Yingyu, Quepen, and Bei Yu—these eight points are used to drain heat from the chest. Qijie, Zusanli, Juxia, Shanglian, and Xialian—these eight points are used to clear heat from the stomach. Yunmen, Yugu, Weizhong, and Suikong—these eight points are used to release heat from the four limbs. Five shu points on each side of the five zang organs—these ten points are used to drain heat in the five zang organs. All these fifty-nine points are sites for treating febrile conditions on both sides of the body. The Emperor asked: When a person is injured by cold, it transforms into heat—why is this so? Qibo said: Cold is yin in nature. If the body's yang qi is not strong enough to resist the invasion of external yin pathogens such as cold, then over time, the stagnation and obstruction caused by cold will hinder normal circulation of blood and qi within the body. As a result, internal heat gradually accumulates due to this blockage. This transformation from cold to heat occurs because prolonged stagnation leads to generation of fire (heat) internally. Qibo said: When cold is excessive, it generates heat. 

62. 調經論 Tiao Jing Lun (Discourse on Regulating the Meridians)

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	調經論:	
黃帝問曰：余聞刺法言，有餘寫之，不足補之，何謂有餘，何謂不足。歧伯對曰：有餘有五，不足亦有五，帝欲何問。帝曰；願盡聞之。歧伯曰：神有餘有不足，氣有餘有不足，血有餘有不足，形有餘有不足，志有餘有不足，凡此十者，其氣不等也。帝曰：人有精氣津液，四支九竅，五藏十六部，三百六十五節，乃生百病，百病之生，皆有虛實，今夫子乃言有餘有五，不足亦有五，何以生之乎。歧伯曰：皆生於五藏也。夫心藏神，肺藏氣，肝藏血，脾藏肉，腎藏志，而此成形，志意通內連骨髓，而成身形五藏，五藏之道，皆出於經隧以行血氣，血氣不和，百病乃變化而生，是故守經隧焉。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I have heard in acupuncture methods that when there is excess, one should drain it; and when there is deficiency, one should tonify it. What does "excess" mean? What does "deficiency" mean? Qibo replied: There are five types of excess, and there are also five types of deficiency. Which one does the Emperor wish to inquire about? The Emperor said; I would like to hear them all. Qibo said: There is excess and deficiency in spirit, there is excess and deficiency in qi, there is excess and deficiency in blood, there is excess and deficiency in form, and there is excess and deficiency in will. In total these ten aspects differ in their conditions. The Emperor said: People have essence, qi, body fluids; four limbs and nine orifices; five zang organs and sixteen parts; 365 joints. From these arise a hundred diseases. The origin of all these hundred diseases involves deficiency and excess. Now you say there are five types of excess and also five types of deficiency—how do they come about? Qibo said: They all arise from the five zang organs. The heart stores the spirit, the lungs store qi, the liver stores blood, the spleen stores flesh, and the kidneys store will. These form the body. Will and intention are internally connected to bone marrow, thus forming the physical form and five zang organs. The pathways of the five zang all emerge from the meridians to circulate blood and qi. When blood and qi are disharmonious, a hundred diseases arise through transformation. Therefore, one must safeguard the meridians.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	調經論:	
帝曰：神有餘不足何如。歧伯曰：神有餘則笑不休，神不足則悲。血氣未并，五藏安定，邪客於形，洒淅起於毫毛，未入於經絡也，故命曰神之微。帝曰：補寫柰何。歧伯曰：神有餘，則寫其小絡之血，出血勿之深斥，無中其大經，神氣乃平。神不足者，視其虛絡，按而致之，刺而利之，無出其血，無泄其氣，以通其經，神氣乃平。帝曰：刺微柰何。歧伯曰：按摩勿釋，著鍼勿斥，移氣於不足，神氣乃得復。
		The Emperor said: What is it like when there is excess or deficiency in spirit? Qibo said: When the spirit is excessive, one laughs without ceasing; when the spirit is deficient, one feels sorrow. When blood and qi have not yet become imbalanced, the five zang remain stable. Pathogenic influences reside in the body, causing a shivering sensation starting at the hair follicles, but they have not entered the meridians or collaterals. Therefore, this is called the subtle manifestation of spirit. The Emperor said: How should one tonify or drain? Qibo said: When the spirit is excessive, drain blood from its small collateral vessels. Do not puncture deeply; do not strike a major meridian—then the spirit and qi will return to balance. When the spirit is deficient, observe its empty collaterals, press to locate them, and needle to promote flow. Do not draw blood; do not release qi—merely unblock the meridians, and the spirit and qi will then return to balance. The Emperor said: How should one needle subtle conditions? Qibo said: Apply massage continuously, and do not forcefully manipulate the needles. Redirect qi to where it is insufficient; then the spirit and qi will recover.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	調經論:	
帝曰：善。有餘不足柰何。歧伯曰：氣有餘則喘欬，上氣不足，則息利少氣。血氣未并，五藏安定，皮膚微病，命曰白氣微泄。帝曰：補寫柰何。歧伯曰：氣有餘，則寫其經隧，無傷其經，無出其血，無泄其氣。不足，則補其經隧，無出其氣。帝曰：刺微柰何。歧伯曰：按摩勿釋，出鍼視之，曰我將深之，適人必革，精氣自伏，邪氣散亂，無所休息，氣泄腠理，真氣乃相得。
		The Emperor said: Well said. What about excess and deficiency? Qibo said: When qi is excessive, there will be panting and coughing; when upper qi is insufficient, breathing becomes shallow with little energy. When blood and qi have not become imbalanced, the five zhang remain stable; only the skin and flesh are slightly affected. This is called a subtle release of white qi. The Emperor said: How should one tonify or drain? Qibo said: When qi is excessive, drain it from the meridian pathways. Do not injure the meridians; do not draw blood; do not exhaust its qi. When there is deficiency, tonify along the meridian pathways without expending its qi. The Emperor said: How should one needle subtle conditions? Qibo said: Apply massage continuously. When removing the needle, observe it and say, "I will go deeper." The patient must then change their state; essence and qi naturally subside, pathogenic influences disperse in confusion, finding no place to rest. Qi is released through the interstices of the skin, allowing true qi to harmonize once more.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	調經論:	
帝曰：善。血有餘不足，柰何。歧伯曰：血有餘則怒，不足則恐。血氣未并，五藏安定，孫絡水溢，則經有留血。帝曰：補寫柰何。歧伯曰：血有餘，則寫其盛經出其血。不足，則視其虛經內鍼其脈中，久留而視，脈大，疾出其鍼，無令血泄。帝曰：刺留血，柰何。歧伯曰：視其血絡，刺出其血，無令惡血得入於經，以成其疾。
		The Emperor said: Well said. What about excess and deficiency of blood? Qibo said: When blood is excessive, one becomes angry; when blood is deficient, one experiences fear. When blood and qi have not combined into imbalance, the five zang remain in harmony; however, if minor collaterals overflow with fluid, then blood may stagnate within the meridians. The Emperor said: How should one tonify or drain? Qibo said: When blood is excessive, drain the overfilled meridians and release the blood. When there is deficiency, observe the deficient meridians and insert needles into their vessels. Leave the needle in place for a while and watch; when the vessel becomes large, quickly remove the needle without allowing blood to escape. The Emperor said: How should one needle stagnant blood? Qibo said: Observe the blood vessels, and needle to remove the stagnated blood. Do not allow foul blood to enter the meridians, lest it cause disease.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	調經論:	
帝曰：善。形有餘不足柰何。歧伯曰：形有餘則腹脹涇溲不利，不足則四支不用。血氣未并，五藏安定，肌肉蠕動，命曰微風。帝曰：補寫柰何。歧伯曰：形有餘則寫其陽經，不足則補其陽絡。帝曰：刺微柰何。歧伯曰：取分肉間，無中其經，無傷其絡，衛氣得復，邪氣乃索。
		The Emperor said: Well said. What about excess and deficiency in form? Qibo said: When the body is excessive, there will be abdominal distension and difficulty with urination and defecation; when deficient, the four limbs become inactive. When blood and qi have not merged into imbalance, the five zang are stable, yet the muscles twitch slightly—this is called "subtle wind." The Emperor said: How should one tonify or drain? Qibo said: When the body is excessive, drain its yang meridians; when deficient, tonify its yang collaterals. The Emperor said: How should one needle subtle conditions? Qibo said: Needle between the muscle layers, without striking a meridian or injuring a collateral. Then defensive qi will recover and pathogenic influences will dissipate.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	調經論:	
帝曰：善。志有餘不足柰何。歧伯曰：志有餘則腹脹飱泄，不足則厥。血氣未并，五藏安定，骨節有動。帝曰：補寫柰何。歧伯曰：志有餘則寫然筋血者，不足則補其復溜。帝曰：刺未并柰何。歧伯曰：即取之，無中其經，邪所乃能立虛。
		The Emperor said: Well said. What about excess and deficiency in will? Qibo said: When the will is excessive, there will be distended abdomen and loose stools; when deficient, one may experience syncope. When blood and qi have not converged into imbalance, the five zang organs remain stable, yet there is movement in the bones and joints. The Emperor said: How should one tonify or drain? Qibo said: When the will is excessive, drain the sinews and blood; when deficient, tonify their return channels. The Emperor said: How should one needle conditions that have not yet converged into imbalance? Qibo said: Needle immediately, without striking the meridians—then pathogenic influences will be unable to establish deficiency.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	調經論:	
帝曰：善。余已聞虛實之形，不知其何以生。歧伯曰：氣血以并，陰陽相傾，氣亂於衛，血逆於經，血氣離居，一實一虛。血并於陰，氣并於陽，故為驚狂。血并於陽，氣并於陰，乃為炅中。血并於上，氣并於下，心煩惋善怒。血并於下，氣并於上，亂而喜忘。帝曰：血并於陰，氣并於陽，如是血氣離居，何者為實，何者為虛。歧伯曰：血氣者，喜溫而惡寒，寒則泣不能流，溫則消而去之。是故氣之所并為血虛，血之所并為氣虛。帝曰：人之所有者，血與氣耳。今夫子乃言血并為虛，氣并為虛，是無實乎。歧伯曰：有者為實，無者為虛，故氣并則無血，血并則無氣，今血與氣相失，故為虛焉。絡之與孫脈俱輸於經，血與氣并，則為實焉。血之與氣并走於上，則為大厥，厥則暴死，氣復反則生，不反則死。帝曰：實者何道從來，虛者何道從去，虛實之要，願聞其故。歧伯曰：夫陰與陽，皆有俞會，陽注於陰，陰滿之外，陰陽勻平，以充其形，九候若一，命曰平人。
		The Emperor said: Well said. I have already heard about the manifestations of deficiency and excess, but I do not know what causes them. Qibo said: When qi and blood converge, yin and yang become imbalanced. Qi becomes chaotic in the defensive aspect; blood flows inversely through the meridians. Blood and qi separate—this results in one being excessive and the other deficient. When blood converges with yin, and qi converges with yang, this leads to startlement and mania. When blood converges with yang and qi converges with yin, it results in internal heat (jiung zhong). When blood accumulates upward and qi accumulates downward, the heart becomes troubled, leading to irritability and a tendency to anger easily. When blood accumulates downward and qi accumulates upward, there is confusion and a tendency to forget things easily. The Emperor said: When blood converges with yin and qi converges with yang in this way, causing separation of blood and qi—what is considered excess, and what is considered deficiency? Qibo said: Blood and qi prefer warmth and dislike cold. Cold causes tears to stagnate and not flow, while warmth disperses and eliminates them. Therefore, where qi converges, blood becomes deficient; where blood converges, qi becomes deficient. The Emperor said: What people possess is merely blood and qi. Now you say that convergence of blood causes deficiency, and convergence of qi also causes deficiency—does this mean there is no excess? Qibo said: Presence indicates excess; absence indicates deficiency. Therefore, when qi converges there is no blood, and when blood converges there is no qi. Now that blood and qi have become separated, this results in deficiency. Collaterals and minor vessels all transport into the meridians; when blood and qi converge, it becomes excess. When blood and qi both surge upward together, this results in major syncope (da jue). When syncope occurs, sudden death may follow. If the qi returns to normal, life is preserved; if it does not return, death will occur. The Emperor said: From which pathways do excess conditions arise? Through which pathways does deficiency depart? I wish to hear the essential principles behind deficiency and excess. Qibo said: Yin and yang both have their respective channels and junctions. Yang flows into yin; when yin is full, it overflows outward. When yin and yang are balanced, they nourish the body's form. The nine pulses appear uniform—this is called a healthy person (ping ren).

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	調經論:	
夫邪之生也，或生於陰，或生於陽。其生於陽者，得之風雨寒暑。其生於陰者，得之飲食居處，陰陽喜怒。帝曰：風雨之傷人柰何。歧伯曰：風雨之傷人也，先客於皮膚，傳入於孫脈，孫脈滿則傳入於絡脈，絡脈滿則輸於大經脈，血氣與邪并客於分腠之閒，其脈堅大，故曰實。實者外堅充滿，不可按之，按之則痛。帝曰：寒濕之傷人柰何。歧伯曰：寒濕之中人也，皮膚不收，肌肉堅緊，榮血泣，衛氣去，故曰虛。虛者聶辟氣不足，按之則氣足以溫之，故快然而不痛。帝曰：善。陰之生實柰何。歧伯曰：喜怒不節，則陰氣上逆，上逆則下虛，下虛則陽氣走之，故曰實矣。帝曰：陰之生虛柰何。歧伯曰：喜則氣下，悲則氣消，消則脈虛空，因寒飲食，寒氣熏滿，則血泣氣去，故曰虛矣。
		Pathogenic influences arise either from yin or from yang. Those that arise from yang are acquired through exposure to wind, rain, cold, and heat. Those that arise from yin are acquired through diet, living conditions, and emotional states such as joy and anger in relation to yin and yang. The Emperor said: How do wind and rain injure people? Qibo said: When wind and rain injure a person, they first reside in the skin, then transmit into the minor vessels. When these are full, they spread to the collateral vessels; when these are also full, they transfer into the major meridians. Pathogenic influences combine with blood and qi between the interstices of muscles and skin, causing the pulse to become firm and large—this is called excess. In cases of excess, the exterior feels solid and full; it cannot be pressed upon, for pressing causes pain. The Emperor said: How do cold and dampness injure people? Qibo said: When cold and dampness invade a person, the skin becomes loose, the muscles become rigid and tense, nutritive blood stagnates, defensive qi departs—this is called deficiency. In cases of deficiency, the skin feels flaccid and the qi is insufficient. When pressed upon, the qi becomes sufficient to warm it, hence a feeling of relief without pain occurs. The Emperor said: Well said. How does excess arise from yin? Qibo said: When joy and anger are not regulated, yin qi rises upward in reversal. This upward reversal causes deficiency below; when there is deficiency below, yang qi surges upward to compensate—this is called excess. Qibo said: When the lower jiao fails to transform and transport, or when there is excessive loss of essence and fluids due to chronic illness or overexertion, yin becomes deficient. This leads to a lack of nourishment for the body's structures and functions—this is called deficiency in yin. Qibo said: Excessive joy causes qi to descend; excessive sorrow causes qi to dissipate. When qi dissipates, the vessels become empty and deficient. Combined with consumption of cold foods and drinks, cold pathogenic influences fill the body, leading to stagnation of blood and departure of qi—this is called deficiency.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	調經論:	
帝曰：經言陽虛則外寒，陰虛則內熱，陽盛則外熱，陰盛則內寒，余已聞之矣，不知其所由然也。歧伯曰：陽受氣於上焦，以溫皮膚分肉之閒。令寒氣在外，則上焦不通，上焦不通，則寒氣獨留於外，故寒慄。帝曰：陰虛生內熱柰何。歧伯曰：有所勞倦，形氣衰少，穀氣不盛，上焦不行，下脘不通，胃氣熱，熱氣熏胸中，故內熱。帝曰：陽盛生外熱柰何。歧伯曰：上焦不通利，則皮膚緻密腠理閉塞，玄府不通，衛氣不得泄越，故外熱。帝曰：陰盛生內寒柰何。歧伯曰：厥氣上逆，寒氣積於胸中而不寫，不寫則溫氣去，寒獨留，則血凝泣，凝則脈不通，其脈盛大以濇，故中寒。
		The Emperor said: The classics state that yang deficiency causes external cold, yin deficiency causes internal heat; yang excess causes external heat, and yin excess causes internal cold. I have already heard this, but do not understand the underlying reasons. Qibo said: Yang receives qi from the upper jiao to warm the spaces between the skin and muscles. When cold pathogenic influences reside externally, the upper jiao becomes obstructed; when the upper jiao is obstructed, cold remains alone on the exterior—thus causing chills. The Emperor said: How does internal heat arise from yin deficiency? Qibo said: When one is overworked and fatigued, the body's form and qi become weakened; grain-derived qi is not abundant. The upper jiao fails to function properly, and the lower 脘 (lower stomach region) becomes obstructed. Stomach qi becomes hot, and this heat ascends to affect the chest—thus causing internal heat. The Emperor said: How does external heat arise from yang excess? Qibo said: When the upper jiao does not function smoothly, the skin becomes tightly closed and the interstices blocked. The xuanfu (pores) become obstructed, preventing defensive qi from being released—thus causing external heat. The Emperor said: How does internal cold arise from yin excess? Qibo said: When rebellious qi rises upward, cold accumulates in the chest without being expelled. If not expelled, warming qi departs and cold remains alone, causing blood to stagnate. Stagnation leads to blocked vessels; the pulse becomes large yet 涩 (congested). Thus internal cold arises.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	調經論:	
帝曰：陰與陽并，血氣以并，病形以成，刺之柰何。歧伯曰：刺此者，取之經隧，取血於營，取氣於衛，用形哉，因四時多少高下。帝曰：血氣以并，病形以成，陰陽相傾，補寫柰何。歧伯曰：寫實者氣盛乃內鍼，鍼與氣俱內，以開其門如利其戶，鍼與氣俱出，精氣不傷，邪氣乃下，外門不閉，以出其疾，搖大其道，如利其路，是謂大寫，必切而出，大氣乃屈。帝曰：補虛柰何。歧伯曰：持鍼勿置，以定其意，候呼內鍼，氣出鍼入，鍼空四塞，精無從去，方實而疾出鍼，氣入鍼出，熱不得還，閉塞其門，邪氣布散，精氣乃得存，動氣候時，近氣不失，遠氣乃來，是謂追之。
		The Emperor said: When yin and yang converge, and blood and qi also converge, disease forms—how should one needle it? Qibo said: To treat this condition, select the meridian pathways; draw blood from the nutritive aspect and extract qi from the defensive aspect. Adjust according to the body's form, taking into account the quantity, depth, and seasonal variations. The Emperor said: When blood and qi have converged and disease has formed, with yin and yang imbalanced—how should one tonify or drain? Qibo said: To drain excess, needle only when the qi is abundant. Insert the needle along with the incoming qi; open its pathway as if opening a door or a gate. Withdraw the needle together with the outgoing qi—this way, essence and qi remain unharmed while pathogenic influences descend. Do not close the exterior pathways to allow disease to exit. Widen the channel as if clearing a road—this is called major drainage (da xie). The needle must be forcefully withdrawn; then the great qi will subside. The Emperor said: How should one tonify deficiency? Qibo said: Hold the needle without releasing it, to stabilize one's intent. Wait for the patient's exhalation before inserting the needle; as qi exits, insert the needle. The four directions around the needle are sealed—essence has no way to escape. When the vessel is full, quickly withdraw the needle while qi enters and the needle leaves. Heat cannot return; close the pathway so pathogenic influences disperse, allowing true essence to remain. Adjust the movement of qi according to timing: near qi does not escape, distant qi arrives—this is called "pursuing" (zui zhi).

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	調經論:	
帝曰：夫子言虛實者有十，生於五藏，五藏五脈耳。夫十二經脈，皆生其病，今夫子獨言五藏，夫十二經脈者，皆絡三百六十五節，節有病，必被經脈，經脈之病，皆有虛實，何以合之。歧伯曰：五藏者，故得六府與為表裏，經絡支節，各生虛實，其病所居，隨而調之。病在脈，調之血。病在血，調之絡。病在氣，調之衛。病在肉，調之分肉。病在筋，調之筋。病在骨，調之骨。燔鍼劫刺其下，及與急者。病在骨，焠鍼藥熨。病不知所痛，兩蹻為上。身形有痛，九候莫病，則繆刺之。痛在於左而右脈病者，巨刺之。必謹察其九候，鍼道備矣。
		The Emperor said: You have mentioned ten aspects of deficiency and excess, arising from the five zang. But there are only five zang and five vessels—how does this work? The twelve primary meridians all give rise to disease. Now you speak only of the five zang, yet each of the twelve meridians connects with 365 joints; when a joint is diseased, it necessarily affects its associated meridian. Since diseases in the meridians also involve deficiency and excess—how can these be reconciled? Qibo said: The five zang have the six fu as their exterior-interior counterparts. Meridians, collaterals, and branch joints each produce deficiency or excess; wherever disease resides, it should be adjusted accordingly. When disease is in the vessels, regulate through blood. When disease is in the blood, regulate through the collateral channels. When disease is in qi, regulate through the defensive aspect (wei). When disease is in muscle, regulate through the intermuscular regions. When disease is in tendons, regulate through the tendons. When disease is in bone, regulate through the bones. Apply moxibustion and rapid needling at points below, as well as on areas of tension. When disease is in bone, use cauterizing needles and medicinal fomentation. When the patient does not know where it hurts, treat both yin and yang meridians as primary. If the body shows pain but no disharmony is found in the nine pulses, then use counter-piercing (miu ci). When pain is on the left but the right pulse shows disease, apply deep needling (ju ci). One must carefully examine the nine pulses; then acupuncture methods are complete. 

63. 繆刺論 Miu Ci Lun

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	繆刺論:	
黃帝問曰：余聞繆刺，未得其意，何謂繆刺。歧伯對曰：夫邪之客於形也，必先舍於皮毛，留而不去，入舍於孫脈，留而不去，入舍於絡脈，留而不去，入舍於經脈，內連五藏，散於腸胃，陰陽俱感，五藏乃傷，此邪之從皮毛而入，極於五藏之次也，如此則治其經焉。今邪客於皮毛，入舍於孫絡，留而不去，閉塞不通，不得入於經，流溢於大絡，而生奇病也。夫邪客大絡者，左注右，右注左，上下左右，與經相干，而布於四末，其氣無常處，不入於經俞，命曰繆刺。帝曰：願聞繆刺，以左取右，以右取左，柰何。其與巨刺何以別之。歧伯曰：邪客於經，左盛則右病，右盛則左病，亦有移易者，左痛未已而右脈先病，如此者，必巨刺之，必中其經，非絡脈也。故絡病者，其痛與經脈繆處，故命曰繆刺。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I have heard of miu ci, but have not grasped its meaning; what is meant by miu ci? Qibo replied: When pathogenic influences reside in the body, they must first dwell in the skin and hair. If they remain without leaving, they will enter into the collateral vessels; if still remaining without leaving, they will then enter into the network vessels; if continuing to linger without departure, they will penetrate into the meridians. Internally connecting with the five zang organs, spreading through the intestines and stomach, affecting both yin and yang, thus injuring the five zang. This is how pathogenic influences, entering from the skin and hair, progress inward to affect the five zang in sequence. In such cases, treatment should be directed at the meridians. Now, when pathogenic influences dwell in the skin and hair and enter into the fine collaterals, if they remain without leaving, blocking and obstructing passage, they cannot penetrate into the meridians but instead overflow into the major collaterals, giving rise to unusual diseases. When pathogenic influences affect the major collaterals, they may move from left to right or from right to left. They spread upward and downward, left and right, intersecting with the meridians, and distribute toward the four extremities. Their qi has no fixed location and does not enter through the regular shu points; this is called miu ci. The Emperor said: I wish to hear about miu ci—treating the right side for a condition on the left, and treating the left side for a condition on the right. How is this done? How does it differ from ju ci? Qibo said: When pathogenic influences dwell in the meridians, if the left side is excessive, it causes illness on the right; if the right side is excessive, it results in illness on the left. There are also cases where this shifts—pain begins on the left but before it subsides, the pulse on the right becomes affected first. In such situations, ju ci must be applied, and needling must precisely strike the meridian involved; these are not collateral vessels. Therefore, when a disease affects the collaterals, its pain is located in a position that contradicts the course of the meridians; thus it is called miu ci.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	繆刺論:	
帝曰：願聞繆刺柰何，取之何如。歧伯曰：邪客於足少陰之絡，令人卒心痛暴脹，胸脇支滿，無積者，刺然骨之前出血，如食頃而已。不已，左取右，右取左，病新發者取五日已。
		The Emperor said: I wish to hear how miu ci is performed, and what method should be used in its application. Qibo said: When pathogenic influences dwell in the collateral of the foot shaoyin, it causes sudden heart pain and acute distension, with fullness extending to the chest and flanks; if there is no accumulation involved, acupuncture should be applied before the ran gu point to release blood. The condition will resolve within a mealtime period. If it does not subside, treat the right side for symptoms on the left and the left side for symptoms on the right; in newly developed conditions, this treatment will resolve the illness within five days.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	繆刺論:	
邪客於手少陽之絡，令人喉痺舌卷，口乾心煩，臂外廉痛，手不及頭，刺手中指次指爪甲上，去端如韭葉各一痏，壯者立已，老者有頃已，左取右，右取左，此新病數日已。
		When pathogenic influences dwell in the collateral network of the hand shaoyang, it causes pharyngeal obstruction and tongue retraction, dry mouth, mental restlessness, pain along the lateral side of the upper arm, and inability to raise the hands to reach the head. Acupuncture should be applied at a point on the nail corner of the second finger of the hand, about as far from the tip as the width of a leek leaf; one needling is sufficient. In robust individuals, symptoms will resolve immediately; in elderly persons, they will subside within a short while. Treat the right side for left-sided symptoms and vice versa. For newly developed conditions lasting several days, this method will bring about recovery.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	繆刺論:	
邪客於足厥陰之絡，令人卒疝暴痛，刺足大指爪甲上與肉交者各一痏，男子立已，女子有頃已，左取右，右取左。
		When pathogenic influences dwell in the network collateral of the foot jueyin, it causes sudden hernia and acute pain. Acupuncture should be performed at a point where the nail corner meets the flesh on the big toe of the foot; one needling suffices. In men, symptoms resolve immediately; in women, they subside within a short time. Treat the right side for conditions on the left and vice versa.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	繆刺論:	
邪客於足太陽之絡，令人頭項肩痛，刺足小指爪甲上，與肉交者各一痏，立已，不已，刺外踝下三痏，左取右，右取左，如食頃已。
		When pathogenic influences dwell in the collaterals of the foot taiyang, it causes pain in the head, neck, and shoulders. Acupuncture should be administered at a point where the nail meets the flesh on the little toe of the foot; one needle insertion will bring immediate relief. If symptoms persist, three needling points should be treated below the lateral malleolus. Treat the right for left-sided pain and vice versa; recovery occurs within a mealtime period.

邪客於手陽明之絡，令人氣滿，胸中喘息，而支胠胸中熱，刺手大指次指爪甲上，去端如韭葉各一痏，左取右，右取左，如食頃已。
		When pathogenic influences dwell in the collagen of the hand yangming, it causes fullness and shortness of breath in the chest, with distension extending to the sides of the chest and heat within the thorax. Acupuncture should be applied on each side at a point located on the nail corner of the index finger, about as far from its tip as the width of leek leaf; one insertion is sufficient. Treat the right for symptoms on the left, or vice versa; relief occurs within a mealtime period.

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	繆刺論:	
邪客於臂掌之閒，不可得屈，刺其踝後，先以指按之痛，乃刺之，以月死生為數，月生一日一痏，二日二痏，十五日十五痏，十六日十四痏。邪客於足陽蹻之脈，令人目痛從內眥始，刺外踝之下半寸所各二痏，左刺右，右刺左，如行十里頃而已。人有所墮墜，惡血留內，腹中滿脹，不得前後，先飲利藥，此上傷厥陰之脈，下傷少陰之絡，刺足內踝之下，然骨之前血脈出血，刺足跗上動脈，不已，刺三毛上各一痏，見血立已，左刺右，右刺左。善悲驚不樂，刺如右方。
		When pathogenic influences dwell between the arm and palm, causing inability to bend the joint, acupuncture should be administered behind the ankle. First press with a finger at the painful spot; then needle it. The number of insertions is determined according to the waxing or waning phase of the moon: on the first day of the new moon, one insertion; on the second day, two insertions; up until the fifteenth day, fifteen insertions; and from the sixteenth day onward, fourteen insertions. When pathogenic influences dwell in the foot yangqiao vessel, it causes eye pain beginning from the inner canthus. Acupuncture should be administered two points at a location half an inch below the lateral malleolus; treat the right side for left-side symptoms and vice versa. Relief occurs within the time it takes to walk ten li. When a person falls and suffers injury, stagnant blood remains internally, causing abdominal fullness and distension with inability to urinate or defecate. First administer purgative medicine. This condition results from upper injury affecting the jueyin meridian and lower injury affecting the shaoyin collateral. Acupuncture should be given at a blood vessel located below the inner ankle, before ran gu, to release blood; also needle the pulsating artery on the dorsum of the foot. If symptoms persist, insert one needle each above the three hairs (on the medial side of the lower leg), and immediate relief will occur upon seeing blood. Treat the right for a left-sided condition and vice versa. For conditions characterized by excessive sadness, startle responses, and lack of joy, acupuncture should be performed according to the method described above.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	繆刺論:	
邪客於手陽明之絡，令人耳聾時不聞音，刺手大指次指爪甲上，去端如韭葉各一痏，立聞，不已，刺中指爪甲上與肉交者，立聞，其不時聞者，不可刺也。耳中生風者，亦刺之如此數，左刺右，右刺左。
		When pathogenic influences dwell in the collar of the hand yangming, they cause deafness and intermittent hearing loss. Acupuncture should be administered on each side at a point on the corner of the nail of the index finger, located about as far from its tip as a leek leaf is wide; one insertion will restore hearing immediately. If symptoms persist, needle the point where the middle finger's nail meets the flesh—immediate restoration of hearing occurs. However, if hearing loss is not intermittent but constant, acupuncture should not be applied. For conditions involving wind affecting the ears, acupuncture should also follow this method; treat the right for symptoms on the left and vice versa.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	繆刺論:	
凡痺往來行無常處者，在分肉閒痛而刺之，以月死生為數用針者，隨氣盛衰以為痏數，針過其日數則脫氣，不及日數則氣不寫，左刺右，右刺左，病已，止，不已，復刺之如法，月生一日一痏，二日二痏，漸多之，十五日十五痏，十六日十四痏，漸少之。
		For all cases of bi syndrome that move about without fixed location, causing pain in the intermuscular spaces and requiring acupuncture treatment, the number of needle insertions should be determined according to the waxing and waning phases of the moon. Adjust the number of needling sessions based on the strength or decline of qi: if too many needles are used beyond the prescribed days, it may cause depletion of qi; if fewer than required, the pathogenic qi will not be fully expelled. Treat the right for pain on the left and vice versa until symptoms resolve. If they persist, repeat treatment according to this method—on the first day after new moon, one insertion per day; two insertions on the second day; gradually increasing up to fifteen insertions by the fifteenth day; then decreasing from the sixteenth day with fourteen insertions, continuing in a gradual decline.

Acupuncture should be administered one insertion at a point on each side where the nail of the second little toe meets the flesh. Treat the right for conditions on the left, or the left for symptoms on the right—disease resolves immediately. If not resolved, repeat treatment according to the same method.

16
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	繆刺論:	
治諸經刺之所過者，不病，則繆刺之。耳聾，刺手陽明，不已，刺其通脈出耳前者。齒齲，刺手陽明，不已，刺其脈入齒中，立已。邪客於五藏之閒，其病也，脈引而痛，時來時止，視其病，繆刺之於手足爪甲上，視其脈，出其血，閒日一刺，一刺不已，五刺已。繆傳引上齒，齒脣寒痛，視其手背脈血者去之，足陽明中指爪甲上大痏，手大指次指爪甲上各一痏，立已，左取右，右取左。
		When treating various meridians, if a condition does not manifest along the course of a particular meridian but still causes symptoms, miu ci should be applied. For deafness, acupuncture should first be administered to the hand yangming meridian; if it does not resolve, needle the collateral vessel that runs in front of the ear. For tooth decay, acupuncture should first be applied to the hand yangming meridian. If it does not resolve, needling the vessel that enters into the teeth will bring immediate relief. When pathogenic influences dwell among the five zang organs and cause disease, it manifests as pain along the pulse lines that comes and goes at irregular intervals. Observe the condition and apply miu ci by needling points on the fingernails or toenails of the hands and feet. Identify the affected vessel and release blood. Perform acupuncture every other day; if symptoms do not resolve after one session, they will be resolved within five sessions. When pathogenic influences cause upward transmission affecting the upper teeth, resulting in coldness and pain in the teeth and lips, observe the blood vessels on the back of the hand and remove stagnation. Apply a major insertion at the nail corner of the middle toe of the foot yangming meridian, and one insertion each at the corresponding points on the index finger's nail corners of both hands; symptoms will resolve immediately. Treat right for left-side conditions or vice versa.

17
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	繆刺論:	
邪客於手足少陰太陰足陽明之絡，此五絡，皆會於耳中，上絡左角，五絡俱竭，令人身脈皆動，而形無知也，其狀若尸，或曰尸厥。刺其足大指內側爪甲上，去端如韭葉，後刺足心，復刺足中指爪上各一痏，後刺手大指內側，去端如韭葉，後刺手心主，少陰銳骨之端各一痏，立已，不已，以竹管吹其兩其，鬄其左角之髮方一寸，燔治飲以美酒一杯，不能飲者灌之，立已。
		When pathogenic influences dwell in the linkages of the hand and foot shaoyin, taiyin, and foot yangming meridians, these five collaterals all converge within the ear, extending upward to connect with the left temple. When all five collaterals are exhausted, it causes movement throughout the body's vessels while rendering the form unaware—its condition resembles that of a corpse; this is sometimes called shi jue (corpse syncope). Needle the inner side of the big toe's nail, about as far from its end as a leek leaf. Then needle the yongquan (KI1) at the sole of the foot and one insertion each on the nail corner of the middle toes. Next, needle the inner side of the thumb of the hand, also about as far from its tip as leek leaf, then needle the shaoxin (HT) meridian's sharp bone end points—each with one insertion; symptoms will resolve immediately. If not resolved, use a bamboo tube to blow air into both ears, cut off hair in an area measuring one cun on the left temple, roast it and mix with one cup of fine wine for consumption. For those unable to drink, administer by infusion; this brings immediate relief.
