顯示相似段落
	繆刺論:	
凡刺之數，先視其經脈，切而從之，審其虛實而調之，不調者經刺之，有痛而經不病者繆刺之，因視其皮部有血絡者盡取之，此繆刺之數也。
		In general, the method of acupuncture involves first observing the meridians and palpating them to follow their course. Carefully determine whether they are deficient or excessive, then regulate accordingly. If regulation is not achieved, apply direct needling along the affected meridian. For conditions with pain but no disease in the meridian itself, miu ci should be used. Additionally, examine the skin regions for visible blood vessels and fully remove stagnation from them—this constitutes the method of miu ci. 

64. 四時刺逆從論 A Discussion on Following and Contravening Seasonal Acupuncture Principles: On the Four Seasons' Influence on Acupuncture Practices

1
打開字典
	四時刺逆... :	
厥陰有餘病陰痺，不足病生熱痺，滑則病狐疝，風濇則病少腹積氣。少陰有餘病皮痺，隱軫不足病肺痺，滑則病肺風疝，濇則病積溲血。太陰有餘病肉痺，寒中不足病脾痺，滑則病脾風疝，濇則病積心腹時滿。陽明有餘病脈痺身時熱，不足病心痺，滑則病心風疝，濇則病積時善驚。太陽有餘病骨痺身重，不足病腎痺，滑則病腎風疝，濇則病積善時巔疾。少陽有餘病筋痺脇滿，不足病肝痺，滑則病肝風疝，濇則病積時筋急目痛。
		Excess in jueyin causes disease of yin bi; deficiency causes disease of sheng re bi. Slipperiness leads to disease of fox hernia, while wind and dryness lead to accumulation of qi in the lower abdomen. Excess in shaoyin causes disease of skin bi; deficiency leads to disease of fei bi. Slipperiness results in disease of lung wind hernia, while dryness causes accumulation and blood in urine. Excess in taiyin causes disease of flesh bi; cold in the middle jiao and deficiency lead to disease of pi bi. Slipperiness causes disease of spleen wind hernia, while dryness results in accumulation with intermittent fullness in the heart and abdomen. Excess in yangming causes disease of pulse bi with occasional body heat; deficiency leads to disease of heart bi. Slipperiness results in heart wind hernia, while dryness produces accumulation and intermittent startle symptoms. Excess in taiyang causes disease of bone bi with body heaviness; deficiency leads to kidney bi. Slipperiness produces kidney wind hernia, while dryness brings about accumulation and intermittent vertigo. Excess in shaoyang causes disease of tendon bi with flank fullness; deficiency leads to liver bi. Slipperiness brings about liver wind hernia, while dryness gives rise to accumulation and intermittent muscle tension with eye pain.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	四時刺逆... :	
是故春氣在經脈，夏氣在孫絡，長夏氣在肌肉，秋氣在皮膚，冬氣在骨髓中。帝曰：余願聞其故。歧伯曰：春者，天氣始開，地氣始泄，凍解冰釋，水行經通，故人氣在脈。夏者，經滿氣溢，入孫絡受血，皮膚充實。長夏者，經絡皆盛，內溢肌中。秋者，天氣始收，腠理閉塞，皮膚引急。冬者蓋藏，血氣在中，內著骨髓，通於五藏。是故邪氣者，常隨四時之氣血而入客也，至其變化不可為度，然必從其經氣，辟除其邪，除其邪，則亂氣不生。
		Therefore, spring qi resides in the meridians and collaterals; summer qi resides in the collateral vessels; long summer qi resides in the muscles; autumn qi resides in the skin; winter qi resides in the bone marrow. The Emperor said: I wish to hear the reason for this. Qibo said: Spring is when the celestial qi begins to open, and the terrestrial qi begins to release; ice melts and water flows through channels. Therefore, human qi resides in the vessels. Summer is when meridians are full and qi overflows, entering the collateral vessels to receive blood, thus filling and strengthening the skin and flesh. Long summer is when both meridians and collateral vessels are full, with qi overflowing into the muscles. Autumn is when celestial qi begins to contract, pores and channels become closed, and the skin becomes tense. Winter is a time of concealment and storage, when blood and qi reside internally, settle in the bone marrow, and connect with the five zang organs. Therefore, pathogenic qi often follows the seasonal qi and blood to invade. Although its transformations are unpredictable, it must follow the meridian qi. By clearing away the pathogenic factors according to this principle, disorderly qi will not arise.

3
打開字典
	四時刺逆... :	
帝曰：逆四時而生亂氣，柰何。歧伯曰：春刺絡脈，血氣外溢，令人少氣。春刺肌肉，血氣環逆，令人上氣。春刺筋骨，血氣內著，令人腹脹。夏刺經脈，血氣乃竭，令人解㑊。夏刺肌肉，血氣內卻，令人善恐。夏刺筋骨，血氣上逆，令人善怒。秋刺經脈，血氣上逆，令人善忘。秋刺絡脈，氣不外行，令人臥不欲動。秋刺筋骨，血氣內散，令人寒慄。冬刺經脈，血氣皆脫，令人目不明。冬刺絡脈，內氣外泄，留為大痺。冬刺肌肉，陽氣竭絕，令人善忘。凡此四時刺者大逆之病，不可不從也，反之則生亂氣相淫病焉。故刺不知四時之經，病之所生，以從為逆，正氣內亂，與精相薄，必審九候，正氣不亂，精氣不轉。
		The Emperor asked: If one goes against the four seasons and generates disordered qi, what should be done? Qibo said: Needling collateral vessels in spring causes blood and qi to overflow externally, resulting in deficiency of qi. Needling the muscles in spring causes blood and qi circulation to become reversed, leading to upward rushing qi. Needling tendons and bones in spring causes blood and qi to stagnate internally, resulting in abdominal distension. Needling meridians in summer exhausts the blood and qi, causing weakness and fatigue. Needling muscles in summer causes blood and qi to retreat internally, leading a person to be easily frightened. Needling tendons and bones during summer causes the blood and qi to rise upward in reversal, making one prone to anger. Needling meridians during autumn causes the blood and qi to move upward in reversal, leading a person to forgetfulness. Needling collateral vessels during autumn prevents qi from moving outward, causing one to lie down and not wish to move. Needling tendons and bones in autumn causes the blood and qi to disperse internally, resulting in chills and shivering. Needling meridians during winter causes the blood and qi both to deplete, resulting in poor eyesight. Needling collateral vessels during winter causes internal qi to leak outward, remaining as a severe bi syndrome. Needling muscles in winter exhausts yang qi completely, leading to forgetfulness. All these acupuncture practices that go against the four seasons are major violations, and must not be followed. If done in reverse, they generate disordered qi and lead to various diseases. Therefore, acupuncture without knowledge of the four seasons' meridians and the origin of diseases turns proper treatment into counteraction. This causes internal disorder in the normal qi, which conflicts with the essence qi. One must carefully examine the nine pulses; only then will the normal qi remain orderly and the essence qi not become disrupted.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	四時刺逆... :	
帝曰：善。刺五藏，中心一日死，其動為噫。中肝五日死，其動為語。中肺三日死，其動為欬。中腎六日死，其動為嚏欠。中脾十日死，其動為吞。刺傷人五藏必死，其動，則依其藏之所變候知其死也。
		The Emperor said: Well said. Needling the five zang organs—if the heart is pierced, death occurs within one day; its movement causes hiccuping. If the liver is pierced, death occurs in five days; its movement results in talking or muttering. If the lung is pierced, death occurs in three days; its movement causes coughing. If the kidney is pierced, death occurs in six days; its movement produces sneezing and yawning. If the spleen is pierced, death occurs in ten days; its movement causes swallowing. Injuring any of the five zang organs through acupuncture will certainly result in death. The symptoms that appear depend on which organ is affected, and by observing these changes, one can determine the time of death. 


65. 標本病傳論 On the Transmission of Diseases: Primary and Secondary Aspects

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	標本病傳... :	
黃帝問曰：病有標本，刺有逆從，柰何。歧伯對曰：凡刺之方，必別陰陽，前後相應，逆從得施，標本相移。故曰：有其在標而求之於標，有其在本而求之於本，有其在本而求之於標，有其在標而求之於本，故治有取標而得者，有取本而得者，有逆取而得者，有從取而得者。故知逆與從，正行無問，知標本者，萬舉萬當，不知標本，是謂妄行。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "Diseases have their primary and secondary aspects; acupuncture has its principles of following or opposing the condition. How should this be understood?" Qibo replied: "In general, the method of acupuncture must distinguish between yin and yang; the front and back should correspond to each other. Only then can the principles of following or opposing be properly applied, allowing the primary and secondary aspects to transform appropriately." Therefore it is said: "Some conditions reside in the secondary aspect and should be treated at the secondary level; some reside in the primary aspect and should be treated there. Some residing in the primary are treated at the secondary, while others residing in the secondary are treated at the primary." Hence, treatment may achieve success by addressing the secondary aspect, or by addressing the primary, or through opposing methods, or through following methods. Therefore, understanding what is to be opposed and what is to be followed allows one to proceed correctly without hesitation. He who understands the primary and secondary aspects will achieve success in every case; he who does not understand them acts recklessly.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	標本病傳... :	
夫陰陽逆從，標本之為道也，小而大，言一而知百病之害，少而多，淺而博，可以言一而知百也。以淺而知深，察近而知遠，言標與本，易而勿及，治反為逆，治得為從。先病而後逆者治其本，先逆而後病者治其本，先寒而後生病者治其本，先病而後生寒者治其本，先熱而後生病者治其本，先熱而後生中滿者治其標，先病而後泄者治其本，先泄而後生他病者治其本，必且調之，乃治其他病，先病而後先中滿者治其標，先中滿而後煩心者治其本。人有客氣有同氣，小大不利治其標，小大利治其本。病發而有餘，本而標之，先治其本，後治其標。病發而不足標而本之，先治其標後治其本。謹察間甚，以意調之，閒者并行，甚者獨行，先小大不利而後生病者治其本。
		The principle of yin-yang, following or opposing, and the relationship between primary and secondary aspects is such that from small one understands large; from a single case one comprehends the harm of all diseases. From few things arise many insights; from shallow knowledge comes broad understanding. Thus, one can speak of one thing and understand a hundred. From shallow knowledge, one can grasp the profound; by observing what is near, one understands what is distant. To speak of the secondary and neglect the primary is easy but insufficient. Treatment that opposes the condition becomes counterproductive; treatment that follows it is in harmony with nature. When the disease occurs first and then becomes counterproductive, treat its primary aspect. When the condition is already counterproductive before the disease arises, also treat its primary aspect. If coldness precedes the onset of illness, treat the primary; if illness comes first followed by coldness, still treat the primary. If heat precedes illness, treat the primary. However, if heat occurs first and is then followed by fullness in the middle jiao (abdomen), treat the secondary aspect. When illness arises before diarrhea, treat its root cause; when diarrhea occurs first and other diseases follow, treat the root of those diseases, but must also regulate them before addressing any additional conditions. If a disease precedes abdominal distension, treat the secondary symptom. If fullness in the abdomen is followed by mental distress or restlessness, then treat the primary aspect. A person may have external pathogenic influences (guest qi) as well as internal disharmonies (same qi). When urination and defecation are both difficult, treat the secondary symptoms; when they are normal or beneficial, treat the primary cause. When a disease manifests with excess and involves both the root and branch aspects, first treat the root, then address the secondary symptoms. When a disease arises in deficiency, involving primarily the secondary aspect with its root, first treat the secondary symptom and later address the primary cause. Carefully observe whether symptoms are mild or severe, and adjust treatment accordingly. For mild conditions, treat multiple aspects simultaneously; for severe ones, focus on a single approach. When urination and defecation become difficult before the onset of disease, treat its primary cause.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	標本病傳... :	
夫病傳者，心病先心痛，一日而欬，三日脇支痛，五日閉塞不通，身痛體重，三日不已，死，冬夜半，夏日中。肺病喘欬，三日而脇支滿痛，一日身重體痛，五日而脹，十日不已，死，冬日入，夏日出。肝病頭目眩脇支滿，三日體重身痛，五日而脹，三日腰脊少腹痛脛痠，三日不已，死，冬日入，夏早食。脾病身痛體重，一日而脹，二日少腹腰脊痛脛痠，三日背𦛗筋痛小便閉，十日不已，死，冬人定，夏晏食。腎病少腹腰脊痛䯒痠，三日背𦛗筋痛小便閉，三日腹脹，三日兩脇支痛，三日不已，死，冬大晨，夏晏晡。胃病脹滿，五日少腹腰脊痛䯒痠，三日背𦛗筋痛小便閉，五日身體重，六日不已，死，冬夜半後，夏日昳。膀胱病小便閉，五日少腹脹腰脊痛䯒痠，一日腹脹，一日身體痛，二日不已，死，冬雞鳴，夏下晡。諸病以次是相傳，如是者，皆有死期。不可刺。閒一藏止，及至三四藏者，乃可刺也。
		When a disease progresses, heart illness begins with chest pain. On the first day, coughing occurs; on the third day, lateral costal region and limb pain develop; by the fifth day, obstruction sets in, causing blockage of channels, body pain, and heaviness. If it persists for three more days without improvement, death will occur—at midnight during winter, or at noon during summer. Lung disease presents with wheezing and cough. On the third day, pain and fullness occur in the lateral costal region; on the first day after that, body heaviness and limb pain appear; by the fifth day further swelling develops. If it continues for ten days without improvement, death occurs—at dusk during winter or at dawn during summer. Liver disease presents with dizziness in the head and eyes, fullness and pain in the lateral costal regions. On the third day body heaviness and bodily pain occur; by the fifth day swelling develops. Three days later lower back, sacrum, and hypogastrium pain appear along with cramping in the shins. If it continues for three more days without recovery, death occurs—at dusk during winter and at early morning mealtime during summer. Spleen disease presents with body pain and heaviness. On the first day swelling occurs; on the second day, lower abdominal, lumbar, and sacral pain along with cramping in shins appear; by the third day, back and shoulder joint pain develop, as well as stiffness in tendons and urinary retention. If it continues for ten more days without improvement, death results—at nightfall during winter or at late morning mealtime during summer. Kidney disease presents with lower abdominal, lumbar and sacral pain as well as cramping in the thighs. On the third day back and shoulder joint pains develop along with stiffness in tendons and retention of urine; by another three days abdominal distension occurs; after a further three days, both lateral costal regions become painful. If it continues for another three days without improvement, death ensues—at early morning during winter or at late afternoon during summer. Stomach disease presents with distension and fullness. On the fifth day lower abdominal, lumbar and sacrum pain along with thigh cramping occur; on the third day after that back and shoulder joint pains appear as well as tendon stiffness and urinary retention; by five more days body heaviness develops. If it continues without improvement for six additional days, death occurs—after midnight during winter or at sunset during summer. Bladder disease presents with urinary retention. On the fifth day, lower abdominal distension and lumbar-sacral pain along with thigh cramps develop; on one more day, abdominal distension appears; after another day, body pains occur. If it continues for two additional days without improvement, death follows—at cockcrow during winter or in late afternoon during summer. All diseases progress according to this sequence; those who follow such patterns all have a determined time of death. Do not perform acupuncture. If the disease has affected only one zang organ and then stops, it is still treatable; but if it progresses to three or four zang organs, acupuncture may be performed. 

66. 天元紀大論 Tianyuan Ji Da Lun

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	天元紀大... :	
黃帝問曰：天有五行，御五位以生寒暑燥濕風，人有五藏，化五氣，以生喜怒思憂恐，論言五運相襲而皆治之，終朞之日，周而復始，余已知之矣，願聞其與三陰三陽之候，柰何合之。鬼臾區稽首再拜對曰：昭乎哉問也。夫五運陰陽者，天地之道也，萬物之綱紀，變化之父母，生殺之本始，神明之府也，可不通乎。故物生謂之化，物極謂之變，陰陽不測謂之神，神用無方謂之聖。夫變化之為用也，在天為玄，在人為道，在地為化，化生五味，道生智，玄生神。神在天為風，在地為木，在天為熱，在地為火，在天為濕，在地為土，在天為燥，在地為金，在天為寒，在地為水，故在天為氣，在地成形，形氣相感而化生萬物矣。然天地者，萬物之上下也，左右者，陰陽之道路也。水火者，陰陽之徵兆也，金木者，生成之終始也。氣有多少，形有盛衰，上下相召，而損益彰矣。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "Heaven has the Five Elements, which govern five positions to produce cold, heat, dryness, dampness, and wind. Humans have the Five Zang organs, which transform into Five Qi to generate joy, anger, thought, sorrow, and fear. The treatise says that the Five Periods succeed each other and all regulate them; on the last day of a full cycle, they begin anew. I already know this. I would like to hear how these relate to the signs of the Three Yin and Three Yang." Guiyuqu kowtowed twice and replied: "What a clear question you have asked!" The Five Periods and Yin-Yang are the principles of Heaven and Earth, the framework for all things, the parents of change and transformation, the origin of life and death, and the repository of spiritual wisdom. How could they not be understood? Therefore, when things are born it is called transformation; when things reach their extremes it is called change. The unpredictability of Yin and Yang is called the divine, and the application of the divine without fixed methods is called sagehood. The function of change and transformation manifests in Heaven as the mysterious, in humans as the Dao, and on Earth as transformation. Transformation gives rise to the Five Flavors; the Dao gives rise to wisdom; the mysterious gives rise to the divine. The divine manifests in Heaven as wind, on Earth as wood; in Heaven as heat, on Earth as fire; in Heaven as dampness, on Earth as earth; in Heaven as dryness, on Earth as metal; in Heaven as cold, on Earth as water. Therefore, in Heaven it is Qi, and on Earth it becomes form. When form and Qi interact, they transform to generate all things. Heaven and Earth are the upper and lower realms of all things; left and right are the pathways of Yin and Yang. Water and fire are the omens of Yin and Yang; metal and wood mark the beginning and end of generation and transformation. Qi varies in quantity, form fluctuates between flourishing and decline. When upper and lower realms call to each other, the losses and gains become evident.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	天元紀大... :	
帝曰：願聞五運之主時也何如。鬼臾區曰：五氣運行，各終朞日，非獨主時也。帝曰：請聞其所謂也。鬼臾區曰：臣積《考太始天元冊》文曰：太虛廖廓，肇基化元，萬物資始，五運終天，布氣真靈，摠統坤元，九星懸朗，七曜周旋，曰陰曰陽，曰柔曰剛，幽顯既位，寒暑弛張，生生化化，品物咸章。臣斯十世，此之謂也。帝曰：善。何謂氣有多少，形有盛衰。鬼臾區曰：陰陽之氣各有多少，故曰三陰三陽也。形有盛衰，謂五行之治，各有太過不及也。故其始也，有餘而往不足隨之，不足而往有餘從之，知迎知隨，氣可與期。應天為天符，承歲為歲直，三合為治。帝曰：上下相召柰何。鬼臾區曰：寒暑燥濕風火，天之陰陽也，三陰三陽上奉之。木火土金水火，地之陰陽也，生長化收藏下應之。天以陽生陰長，地以陽殺陰藏。天有陰陽，地亦有陰陽。木火土金水火，地之陰陽也，生長化收藏。故陽中有陰，陰中有陽。所以欲知天地之陰陽者，應天之氣動而不息，故五歲而右遷，應地之氣靜而守位，故六朞而環會。動靜相召，上下相臨，陰陽相錯，而變由生也。
		The Emperor said: "I would like to hear about how the Five Periods govern the seasons." Guiyuqu said: "The Five Qi operate in cycles, each completing a full cycle on its final day. They do not govern the seasons alone." The Emperor said: "Please explain what you mean by that." Guiyuqu said: "I have compiled the text from the 'Kao Taishi Tianyuan Ce' which states: The great void is vast and boundless, establishing the foundation of transformation. All things originate from this; the Five Periods extend through Heaven, distributing true spiritual Qi to oversee the fundamental Earth. Nine stars hang bright, seven luminaries revolve in cycles. They are called Yin and Yang, soft and firm. When hidden and manifest have their places, cold and heat relax and tighten. Life gives rise to transformation, and all things display their forms." "I, through ten generations of my lineage, speak of this very thing." The Emperor said: "Good." What is meant by the varying amounts of Qi, and the waxing and waning of form? Guiyuqu said: "The Yin-Yang Qi each have varying amounts; hence, they are called the Three Yin and Three Yang." The waxing and waning of forms refers to the governance of the Five Elements, each having either excessive or insufficient influence. Therefore, at their beginning, when there is excess, deficiency follows; when there is deficiency, excess follows. Knowing how to meet and follow these changes, one can anticipate the Qi's movements. Responding to Heaven is called Tianfu; aligning with the year is called Suizhi; and when three harmonize, it is considered effective governance. The Emperor asked: "What does it mean for upper and lower to call to each other?" Guiyuqu said: "Cold, heat, dryness, damp, wind, and fire are the Yin-Yang of Heaven; the Three Yin and Three Yang respond to them from below." Wood, fire, earth, metal, water, and fire are the Yin-Yang on Earth; they manifest as growth, flourishing, transformation, harvesting, and storage, responding from below. Heaven uses Yang to generate and Yin to grow; Earth uses Yang to kill and Yin to store. Heaven has Yin and Yang, and Earth also has Yin and Yang. Wood, fire, earth, metal water, and fire are the yin and yang of Earth; they manifest in growth, flourishing, transformation, harvest, and storage. Therefore, within Yang there is Yin, and within Yin there is Yang. Therefore, to understand the Yin and Yang of Heaven and Earth: The Qi responding to Heaven moves ceaselessly; thus, every five years it shifts rightward. The Qi responding to Earth remains still and holds its position; therefore, after six cycles it completes a full gathering. When movement and stillness call to each other, upper and lower realms meet, Yin and Yang interweave, change arises from this.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	天元紀大... :	
帝曰：上下周紀，其有數乎。鬼臾區曰：天以六為節，地以五為制。周天氣者，六朞為一備，終地紀者，五歲為一周。君火以明，相火以位。五六相合而七百二十氣，為一紀，凡三十歲，千四百四十氣，凡六十歲，而為一周，不及太過，斯皆見矣。帝曰：夫子之言，上終天氣，下畢地紀，可謂悉矣。余願聞而藏之，上以治民，下以治身，使百姓昭著，上下和親，德澤下流，子孫無憂，傳之後世，無有終時，可得聞乎。鬼臾區曰：至數之機，迫迮以微，其來可見，其往可追，敬之者昌，慢之者亡，無道行私，必得天殃，謹奉天道，請言真要。帝曰：善言始者，必會於終，善言近者，必知其遠，是則至數極而道不惑，所謂明矣。願夫子推而次之，令有條理，𥳑而不匱，久而不絕，易用難忘，為之綱紀，至數之要，願盡聞之。鬼臾區曰：昭乎哉問，明乎哉道，如鼓之應桴，響之應聲也。臣聞之，甲己之歲，土運統之。乙庚之歲，金運統之。丙辛之歲，水運統之。丁壬之歲，木運統之。戊癸之歲，火運統之。帝曰：其於三陰三陽，合之柰何。鬼臾區曰：子午之歲，上見少陰。丑未之歲，上見太陰。寅申之歲，上見少陽。卯酉之歲，上見陽明。辰戌之歲，上見太陽。巳亥之歲，上見厥陰。少陰，所謂標也，厥陰，所謂終也。厥陰之上，風氣主之。少陰之上，熱氣主之。太陰之上，濕氣主之。少陽之上，相火主之。陽明之上，燥氣主之。太陽之上，寒氣主之。所謂本也，是謂六元。帝曰：光乎哉道，明乎哉論，請著之玉版，藏之金匱，署曰天元紀。
		The Emperor asked: "Is there a numerical pattern in the cyclical order of the upper and lower realms?" Guiyuqu said: "Heaven uses six as its cycle, Earth uses five as its regulation." The full cycle of Heaven's Qi is completed in six cycles. The completion of Earth's cycle occurs every five years. The sovereign fire illuminates, the ministerial fire maintains its position. When five and six combine, they produce 720 Qi, forming one cycle. In total, thirty years; 1440 Qi in all, totaling sixty years to complete a full cycle. The insufficiencies and excesses are thus clearly seen. The Emperor said: "Your words, Master, reach from the uppermost Heaven's Qi to the completion of Earth's cycles. You have explained it thoroughly." I wish to hear and preserve this knowledge, using it above to govern the people and below to regulate my own body. This will make the common people enlightened and harmonious, foster unity between ruler and subject, allow virtue and grace to flow downward, ensure that descendants have no worries, and pass these teachings on through future generations without end. May I hear this? Guiyuqu said: "The key to the ultimate numbers is subtle and difficult to grasp. Its coming can be foreseen, its going can be traced back. Those who respect it will flourish; those who neglect it will perish. To act without principle for selfish ends will surely bring heavenly calamity. With reverence for Heaven's way, I shall now speak of the true essentials." The Emperor said: "Those who speak well of the beginning must understand its end; those who speak well of what is near must know what is far. This means that when one reaches the ultimate numbers, the Dao becomes clear—this is called enlightenment." I wish, Master, that you would elaborate and arrange it in order, so that it is well-organized, never exhausted, enduring without interruption, easy to use and hard to forget. Let it serve as a framework for governance. As for the essentials of the ultimate numbers, I wish to hear them all. Guiyuqu said: "What a clear question! What an enlightened Dao! It is like the drum responding to the mallet, or the echo answering the sound." I have heard that in years Jia and Ji, the Earth Period governs. In years Yi and Geng, the Metal Period governs. In years Bing and Xin, the Water Period governs. In years Ding and Ren, the Wood Period governs. In years Wu and Gui, the Fire Period governs. The Emperor asked: "How do these relate to the Three Yin and Three Yang?" Guiyuqu said: "In years Zi and Wu, Shaoyin is seen above." In years Chou and Wei, Taiyin is seen above. In years Yin and Shen, Shaoyang is seen above. In years Mao and You, Yangming is seen above. In years Chen and Xu, Taiyang is seen above. In years Si and Hai, Jueyin is seen above. Shaoyin is called the "beginning," and Jueyin is called the "end." Above Jueyin, Wind Qi governs. Above Shaoyin, Heat Qi governs. Above Taiyin, Dampness Qi governs. Above Shaoyang, Ministerial Fire governs. Above Yangming, Dryness Qi governs. Above Taiyang, Cold Qi governs. This is called the "root," and these are known as the Six Fundamentals. The Emperor said: "How luminous is this Dao! How clear is this discourse! Please inscribe it on jade tablets, store it in golden cabinets, and title it 'Tianyuan Ji'." 

67. 五運行大論 Great Discourse on the Five Movements

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五運行大... :	
黃帝坐明堂，始正天綱，臨觀八極，考建五常，請天師而問之曰：論言天地之動靜，神明為之紀，陰陽之升降，寒暑彰其兆。余聞五運之數於夫子，夫子之所言，正五氣之各主歲爾，首甲定運，余因論之。鬼臾區曰：土主甲己，金主乙庚，水主丙辛，木主丁壬，火主戊癸。子午之上，少陰主之。丑未之上，太陰主之。寅申之上，少陽主之。卯酉之上，陽明主之。辰戌之上，太陽主之。巳亥之上，厥陰主之。不合陰陽，其故何也。歧伯曰：是明道也，此天地之陰陽也。夫數之可數者，人中之陰陽也，然所合數之可得者也。夫陰陽者，數之可十，推之可百，數之可千，推之可萬。天地陰陽者，不以數推以象之謂也。
		The Yellow Emperor, seated in the Hall of Brightness, first established the heavenly principles. He observed the eight extremes and examined the Five Constants. He then invited the Heavenly Teacher to ask him: "Discourses say that the movements and stillness of heaven and earth are governed by spirits and wisdom; the rising and falling of yin and yang manifest signs through cold and heat." I have heard from my teacher about the five movements and their numbers. What my teacher said was merely to establish that each of the Five Qi presides over a particular year; beginning with the jia year determines the movement, and I therefore discussed it further. Guiyuqu said: "Earth presides over jia and ji, Metal presides over yi and geng, Water presides over bing and xin, Wood presides over ding and ren, Fire presides over wu and gui." On the days of zi and wu, Shaoyin governs. On the days of chou and wei, Taiyin governs. On the days of yin and shen, Shaoyang governs. On the days of mao and you, Yangming governs. On the days of chen and xu, Taiyang governs. On the days of si and hai, Jueyin governs. Why do they not conform to yin and yang? Qibo said: "This is the clear principle; these are the yin and yang of heaven and earth." The numbers that can be counted are the yin and yang within humanity, yet they correspond to the numbers that can indeed be obtained. Yin and Yang are such that when counted, they reach ten; when extended, a hundred; when counted further, a thousand; and when extended still more, ten thousand. The yin and yang of heaven and Earth are not determined by numerical calculation but by their images or manifestations.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五運行大... :	
帝曰：願聞其所始也。歧伯曰：昭乎哉問也。臣覽《太始天元冊》文，丹天之氣經于牛女戊分，黅天之氣經于心尾己分，蒼天之氣經于危室柳鬼，素天之氣經于亢氐昴畢，玄天之氣經于張翼婁胃。所謂戊己分者，奎壁角軫，則天地之門戶也。夫候之所始，道之所生，不可不通也。
		The Emperor said: "I wish to hear about its beginning." Qibo said: "What a clear and profound question!" Min read the text of the "Taishi Tianyuan Ce" and said: The red heaven's qi passes through Niu, Nü, and wu fen; the yellow heaven's qi passes through Xin, Wei, and ji fen; the green heaven's qi passes through Wei, Shi, Liu, and Gui; the white heaven's qi passes through Kang, Di, Mao, and Bi; the black heaven's qi passes through Zhang, Yi, Lou, and Wei. What is referred to as wu ji fen are Kui, Bi, Jiao, and Zhen; these are the gates of heaven and earth. The beginning of observation is where the Dao originates; it cannot be left ununderstood.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五運行大... :	
帝曰：善論言天地者，萬物之上下，左右者，陰陽之道路，未知其所謂也。歧伯曰：所謂上下者，歲上下見陰陽之所在也。左右者，諸上見厥陰，左少陰右太陽。見少陰，左太陰右厥陰。見太陰，左少陽右少陰。見少陽，左陽明右大陰。見陽明，左太陽右少陽。見太陽，左厥陰右陽明。所謂面北而命其位，言其見也。帝曰：何謂下。歧伯曰：厥陰在上則少陽在下，左陽明右太陰。少陰在上則陽明在下，左太陽右少陽。太陰在上則太陽在下，左厥陰右陽明。少陽在上則厥陰在下，左少陰右太陽。陽明在上則少陰在下，左太陰右厥陰。太陽在上則太陰在下，左少陽右少陰。所謂面南而命其位，言其見也。上下相遘，寒暑相臨，氣相得則和，不相得則病。帝曰：氣相得而病者，何也。歧伯曰：以下臨上，不當位也。帝曰：動靜何如。歧伯曰：上者右行，下者左行，左右周天餘而復會也。帝曰：余聞鬼臾區曰：應地者靜。今夫子乃言下者左行，不知其所謂也，願聞何以生之乎。歧伯曰：天地動靜，五行遷復，雖鬼臾區其上候而已，猶不能徧明。夫變化之用，天垂象，地成形，七曜緯虛，五行麗地。地者，所以載生成之形類也。虛者，所以列應天之精氣也。形精之動，猶根本之與枝葉也，仰觀其象，雖遠可知也。
		The Emperor said: "Good discussions about heaven and earth speak of the upper and lower, left and right aspects of all things; these are the pathways of yin and yang. I do not yet know what is meant by this." Qibo said: "What is referred to as upper and lower means the positions of yin and yang in terms of years, seen from above and below." Left and right refer to the following: among those above, Jueyin is present; on the left is Shaoyin, and on the right is Taiyang. When Shaoyin appears, on the left is Taiyin and on the right is Jueyin. When Taiyin appears, on the left is Shaoyang and on the right is Shaoyin. When Shaoyang appears, on the left is Yangming and on the right is Taiyin. When Yangming appears, on the left istaiyang and on the right isshaoyang. When Taiyang appears, on the left is Jueyin and on the right is Yangming. What is meant by facing north to assign their positions is a description of where they appear. The Emperor said: "What is meant by 'below'?" Qibo said: "When Jueyin is above, Shaoyang is below; on the left is Yangming, and on the right istaiyin." Whenshaoyin is above,Yangming is below; on theleft istaiyang, and on theright isshaoyang. Whentaiyin is above,Taiyang is below; on the leftisjueyin, and on therightisyangming. Whenshaoyang is above,Jueyin is below; on the leftishaoyin, and on therighthastaiyang. Whenyangming is above,Shaoyin is below; ontheleft istaiyin, andontherightisjueyin. Whentaiyang is above,Taiyin is below; onthe left isshaoyang, andon therightishaoyin. What is meant by facing south to assign their positions is a statement of where they appear. When upper and lower meet, cold and heat approach each other; when the qi harmonize with one another, there is harmony; if they do not harmonize, illness occurs. The Emperor said: "Why would disease occur even when the qi are in harmony?" Qibo said: "When what is below approaches what is above improperly, it does not occupy its proper position." The Emperor said: "What about movement and stillness?" Qibo said: "That which is above moves to the right, that which is below moves to the left; after moving around the heavens for a cycle with some remainder, they reunite." The Emperor said: "I have heard Guiyuqu say: 'That which corresponds to Earth is still.'" Now you, my teacher, speak of that which is below moving to the left. I do not understand what this means; I wish to know how it arises. Qibo said: "The movements and stillness of heaven and Earth, the shifting and returning of the Five Elements—although Guiyuqu merely observed their upper signs, he could not fully illuminate them." The function of change and transformation is manifested as the heavens display images, and Earth forms shapes; the seven luminaries weave through the void, while the Five Elements attach to Earth. Earth is what carries and gives form to all things that are generated and formed. The void is where the refined qi corresponding to heaven's manifestations are arranged. The movement of form and essence is like that of roots and branches; by looking upward at their images, even distant things can be known.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五運行大... :	
帝曰：地之為下，否乎。歧伯曰：地為人之下，太虛之中者也。帝曰：馮乎。歧伯曰：大氣舉之也。燥以乾之，暑以蒸之，風以動之，濕以潤之，寒以堅之，火以溫之。故風寒在下，燥熱在上，濕氣在中，火遊行其閒，寒暑六入，故令虛而生化也。故燥勝則地乾，暑勝則地熱，風勝則地動，濕勝則地泥，寒勝則地裂，火勝則地固矣。
		The Emperor said: "Is Earth truly below?" Qibo said: "Earth is beneath humanity, yet it exists within the great void." The Emperor said: "What supports it?" Qibo said: "It is upheld by the great qi." Dryness dries it, heat steams it, wind moves it, dampness moistens it, cold hardens it, and fire warms it. Therefore, wind and cold are below, dryness and heat above, dampness in the middle, while fire moves among them. Cold and heat enter six times, thus causing emptiness where generation and transformation occur. Therefore, when dryness prevails, the earth becomes arid; when heat prevails, the earth grows hot; when wind prevails, the earth shakes; when dampness prevails, the ground turns muddy; when cold prevails, the earth cracks; and when fire prevails, the earth solidifies.

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五運行大... :	
帝曰：天地之氣，何以候之。歧伯曰：天地之氣，勝復之作，不形於診也。《脈法》曰：天地之變，無以脈診，此之謂也。帝曰：閒氣何如。歧伯曰：隨氣所在，期於左右。帝曰：期之柰何。歧伯曰：從其氣則和，違其氣則病，不當其位者病，迭移其位者病，失守其位者危，尺寸反者死，陰陽交者死。先立其年，以知其氣，左右應見，然後乃可以言死生之逆順。
		The Emperor said: "How can one observe the qi of heaven and Earth?" Qibo said: "The qi of heaven and earth, in their patterns of dominance and restoration, do not manifest in diagnosis." "The Pulse Method" says: "Changes in heaven and earth cannot be diagnosed through pulse examination"—this is what it means. The Emperor said: "What about the intermittent qi?" Qibo said: "It follows where the qi resides, and its timing is determined by left or right." The Emperor said: "How can one determine this timing?" Qibo said: "Following its qi brings harmony; going against it causes illness. When the qi does not occupy its proper position, there is disease. When positions shift repeatedly, there is illness. When a position is completely abandoned, danger arises. When cun and chi are reversed, death occurs. When yin and yang intermingle, death follows." First establish the year to know its qi; when responses appear on the left and right accordingly, only then can one speak of the favorable or adverse outcomes for life and death.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五運行大... :	
帝曰：寒暑燥濕風火，在人合之柰何，其於萬物，何以生化。歧伯曰：東方生風，風生木，木生酸，酸生肝，肝生筋，筋生心。其在天為玄，在人為道，在地為化。化生五味，道生智，玄生神，化生氣。神在天為風，在地為木，在體為筋，在氣為柔，在藏為肝。其性為暄，其德為和，其用為動，其色為蒼，其化為榮，其蟲毛，其政為散，其令宣發，其變摧拉，其眚為隕，其味為酸，其志為怒。怒傷肝，悲勝怒，風傷肝，燥勝風，酸傷筋，辛勝酸。
		The Emperor said: "How do cold, heat, dryness, dampness, wind, and fire correspond to humanity? And how do they give rise to generation and transformation in all things?" Qibo said: "In the East, wind is born; wind gives rise to Wood; Wood produces sour flavor; sour flavor nourishes the Liver; the Liver generates tendons; and tendons give rise to the Heart." In heaven it is xuan, in humanity it is Dao, on Earth it is transformation. Transformation gives rise to the five flavors; Dao produces wisdom; xuan produces spirit; and transformation generates qi. Spirit, in heaven is wind, on Earth is Wood, in the body is tendons, in terms of qi is softness, and among the zang organs corresponds to the Liver. Its nature is warmth, its virtue harmony, its function movement, its color green, its transformation flourishing, its creature hairy, its administration dispersion, its command spreading and releasing, its change breaking and pulling apart, its calamity falling, its flavor sour, and its emotion anger. Anger harms the Liver; sorrow overcomes anger. Wind harms the Liver; dryness overcomes wind. Sour flavor harms tendons; pungent flavor overcomes sour.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五運行大... :	
南方生熱，熱生火，火生苦，苦生心，心生血，血生脾。其在天為熱，在地為火，在體為脈，在氣為息，在藏為心。其性為暑，其德為顯，其用為躁，其色為赤，其化為茂，其蟲羽，其政為明，其令鬱蒸，其變炎爍，其眚燔焫，其味為苦，其志為喜。喜傷心，恐勝喜，熱傷氣，寒勝熱，苦傷氣，鹹勝苦。
		In the South, heat is born; heat gives rise to Fire; Fire produces bitter flavor; bitter flavor nourishes the Heart; the Heart generates blood; and blood gives rise to the Spleen. In heaven it is heat, on Earth it is Fire, in the body it is vessels (pulse), in terms of qi it is respiration, and among the zang organs it corresponds to the Heart. Its nature is heat, its virtue manifestation, its function restlessness, its color red, its transformation flourishing growth, its creature winged, its administration clarity, its command stagnation and steaming, its change blazing and scorching, its calamity burning and destruction, its flavor bitter, and its emotion joy. Joy harms the Heart; fear overcomes joy. Heat harms qi; cold overcomes heat. Bitter flavor harms qi; salty flavor overcomes bitter.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五運行大... :	
中央生濕，濕生土，土生甘，甘生脾，脾生肉，肉生肺。其在天為濕，在地為土，在體為肉，在氣為充，在藏為脾。其性靜兼，其德為濡，其用為化，其色為黃，其化為盈，其蟲倮。其政為謐，其令雲雨，其變動注，其眚淫潰，其味為甘，其志為思。思傷脾，怒勝思，濕傷肉，風勝濕，甘傷脾，酸勝甘。
		In the Center, dampness is born; dampness gives rise to Earth; Earth produces sweet flavor; sweet flavor nourishes the Spleen; the Spleen generates flesh; and flesh gives rise to the Lungs. In heaven it is dampness, on Earth it is Earth, in the body it is flesh, in terms of qi it is fullness, and among the zang organ corresponds to the Spleen. Its nature is stillness and integration, its virtue moistening, its function transformation, its color yellow, its transformation fullness, its creature hairless. Its administration is quietude, its command clouds and rain, its change movement and dripping, its calamity excessive discharge and collapse, its flavor sweet, and its emotion thoughtfulness. Thoughtfulness harms the Spleen; anger overcomes thoughtfulness. Dampness harms flesh; wind overcomes dampness. Sweet flavor harms the Spleen; sour flavor overcomes sweet.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五運行大... :	
西方生燥，燥生金，金生辛，辛生肺，肺生皮毛，皮毛生腎。其在天為燥，在地為金，在體為皮毛，在氣為成，在藏為肺，其性為涼，其德為清，其用為固，其色為白，其化為斂，其蟲介，其政為勁，其令霧露，其變肅殺，其眚蒼落，其味為辛，其志為憂。憂傷肺，喜勝憂，熱傷皮毛，寒勝熱，辛傷皮毛，苦勝辛。
		In the West, dryness is born; dryness gives rise to Metal; Metal produces pungent flavor; pungent flavor nourishes the Lungs; the Lungs generate skin and hair; and skin and hair give rise to the Kidneys. In heaven it is dryness, on Earth it is Metal, in the body it is skin and hair, in terms of qi completion, among the zang organs corresponds to Lungs. Its nature is coolness, its virtue clarity, its function consolidation, its color white, its transformation contraction, its creature shelled, its administration firmness, its command mist and dew, its change dryness and killing, its calamity withering and falling, its flavor pungent, and its emotion sorrow. Sorrow harms the Lungs; joy overcomes sorrow. Heat harms skin and hair; cold overcomes heat. Pungent flavor harms skin and hair; bitter flavor overcomes pungent.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五運行大... :	
北方生寒，寒生水，水生鹹，鹹生腎，腎生骨髓，髓生肝。其在天為寒，在地為水，在體為骨，在氣為堅，在藏為腎，其性為凜，其德為寒，其用為，其色為黑，其化為肅，其蟲鱗，其政為靜，其令，其變凝冽，其眚冰雹，其味為鹹，其志為恐。恐傷腎，思勝恐，寒傷血，燥勝寒，鹹傷血，甘勝鹹。
		In the North, cold is born; cold gives rise to Water; Water produces salty flavor; salty flavor nourishes the Kidneys; the Kidneys generate bone marrow; and bone marrow gives rise to the Liver. In heaven it is cold, on Earth it is Water, in the body it is bone, in terms of qi firmness, among the zang organs corresponding to Kidneys. Its nature is severity, its virtue cold, its function solidity, its color black, its transformation dryness and contraction, its creature scaled, its administration stillness, its command unspecified, its change freezing and piercing cold, its calamity hail and ice, its flavor salty, and its emotion fear. Fear harms the Kidneys; thoughtfulness overcomes fear. Cold harms blood; dryness overcomes cold. Salty flavor harms blood; sweet flavor overcomes salty.

11
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五運行大... :	
氣更立，各有所先，非其位則邪，當其位則正。帝曰：病生之變何如。歧伯曰：氣相得則微，不相得則甚。帝曰：主歲何如。歧伯曰：氣有餘，則制己所勝而侮所不勝，其不及，則己所不勝侮而乘之，己所勝輕而侮之。侮反受邪，侮而受邪，寡於畏也。帝曰：善。
		The qi changes and establishes itself anew, each having its own precedence. When it does not occupy the proper position, it becomes pathogenic; when in the correct position, it is normal. The Emperor said: "What are the changes that arise from disease?" Qibo said: "When the qi harmonize, the condition is mild; when they do not harmonize, it becomes severe." The Emperor said: "What about those who preside over each year?" Qibo said: "When the qi is excessive, it controls what it normally overcomes and belittles what it does not overcome. When deficient, what it cannot overcome will then belittle and attack it; while what it usually overcomes becomes light and mocks it." To be belittled is to invite pathogenic influences. To be belittled and yet receive them means a lack of fear or respect. The Emperor said: "Good." 

68. 六微旨大論 Great Discourse on the Six Subtle Essentials

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六微旨大... :	
黃帝問曰：嗚呼遠哉，天之道也，如迎浮雲，若視深淵，視深淵尚可測，迎浮雲莫知其極。夫子數言謹奉天道，余聞而藏之，心私異之，不知其所謂也。願夫子溢志盡言其事，令終不滅，久而不絕，天之道可得聞乎。歧伯稽首再拜對曰：明乎哉問天之道也，此因天之序盛衰之時也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "Alas, how remote is the way of Heaven! It is like greeting a drifting cloud, or gazing into a deep abyss. Even looking into a deep abyss can still be measured, but greeting a drifting cloud gives no knowledge of its limit." The Master often spoke about carefully following the Way of Heaven. I have heard these words and kept them in mind, secretly finding them remarkable, but I do not understand what they mean. I wish the Master would fully express his thoughts and speak in detail about these matters, so that they may never be lost or forgotten over time. May I then hear of the Way of Heaven? Qibo bowed respectfully twice and replied: "How clear is your question about the Way of Heaven! This concerns understanding the order of Heaven, as well as the times of flourishing and decline."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六微旨大... :	
帝曰：願聞天道六六之節盛衰何也。歧伯曰：上下有位，左右有紀。故少陽之右，陽明治之。陽明之右，太陽治之。太陽之右，厥陰治之。厥陰之右，少陰治之。少陰之右，太陰治之。太陰之右，少陽治之。此所謂氣之標，蓋南面而待也。故曰：因天之序，盛衰之時，移光定位，正立而待之，此之謂也。少陽之上，火氣治之，中見厥陰。陽明之上，燥氣治之，中見太陰。太陽之上，寒氣治之，中見少陰。厥陰之上，風氣治之，中見少陽。少陰之上，熱氣治之，中見太陽。太陰之上，濕氣治之，中見陽明。所謂本也。本之下，中之見也。見之下，氣之標也。本標不同，氣應異象。
		The Emperor said: "I wish to hear about the six-by-six cycles of the Way of Heaven, what they signify in terms of flourishing and decline." Qibo said: "Above and below have their positions; left and right have their principles." Therefore, to the right of Shaoyang, Yangming governs. To the right of Yangming, Taiyang governs. To the right of Taiyang, Jueyin governs. To the right of Jueyin, Shaoyin governs. To the right of Shaoyin, Taiyin governs. To the right of Taiyin, Shaoyang governs. This is what is meant by the outward manifestation of qi; it is as if one faces south and waits. Therefore, it is said: "Following the order of Heaven, understanding times of flourishing and decline, shifting light to establish position, standing upright and waiting—this is what it means." Above Shaoyang, the Fire qi governs; internally, Jueyin is seen. Above Yangming, the Dryness qi governs; internally, Taiyin is seen. Above Taiyang, the Cold qi governs; internally, Shaoyin is seen. Above Jueyin, the Wind qi governs; internally, Shaoyang is seen. Above Shaoyin, the Heat qi governs; internally, Taiyang is seen. Above Taiyin, the Dampness qi governs; internally, Yangming is seen. This is what is called the root. Beneath the root, one sees the intermediate aspect. Beneath this intermediate aspect lies the outward manifestation of qi. The root and the manifestation are different; thus, the qi responds with different phenomena.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六微旨大... :	
帝曰：其有至而至，有至而不至，有至而太過，何也。歧伯曰：至而至者和。至而不至，來氣不及也。未至而至，來氣有餘也。帝曰：至而不至，未至而至，如何。歧伯曰：應則順，否則逆，逆則變生，變則病。帝曰：善。請言其應。歧伯曰：物，生其應也。氣，脈其應也。
		The Emperor asked: "Some arrive on time and do so, some arrive but fail to fulfill their function, and others arrive in excess—why is this?" Qibo said: "Those that arrive as expected are harmonious." Those that arrive but do not fulfill their function indicate the incoming qi is insufficient. Those that arrive before expected indicate the incoming qi is excessive. The Emperor asked: "What are the implications of those who arrive but do not fulfill, and those who arrive before they should?" Qibo said: "If it corresponds, then the condition is harmonious; if not, it becomes disharmonious. Disharmony leads to change, and change gives rise to illness." The Emperor said: "Well said." Please explain their corresponding manifestations. Qibo said: "Things in nature respond accordingly." Qi, the pulse responds to it.

4
打開字典
	六微旨大... :	
帝曰：善。願聞地理之應六節氣位何如。歧伯曰：顯明之右，君火之位也。君火之右，退行一步，相火治之，復行一步，土氣治之，復行一步，金氣治之，復行一步，水氣治之，復行一步，木氣治之，復行一步，君火治之。相火之下，水氣承之。水位之下，土氣承之。土位之下，風氣承之。風位之下，金氣承之。金位之下，火氣承之。君火之下，陰精承之。帝曰：何也。歧伯曰：亢則害，承迺制，制則生化，外列盛衰，害則敗亂，生化大病。
		The Emperor said: "Well said." I wish to hear how the six seasonal qi positions correspond to geographical locations. Qibo said: "To the right of Xianming is the position of the sovereign Fire." To the right of the sovereign Fire, one step back is occupied by the ministerial Fire. One more step forward, Earth qi governs; another step forward, Metal qi governs; yet another step forward, Water qi governs; still another step forward, Wood qi governs; and finally, one more step forward returns to the sovereign Fire governing it. Beneath the ministerial Fire, Water qi supports it. Beneath the position of Water, Earth qi supports it. Beneath the position of Earth, Wind qi supports it. Beneath the position of Wind, Metal qi supports it. Beneath the position of Metal, Fire qi supports it. Beneath the sovereign Fire, yin essence supports it. The Emperor asked: "Why is this so?" Qibo said: "Excess leads to harm; support brings regulation. Regulation allows for generation and transformation, manifesting externally as cycles of flourishing and decline. Harm results in disorder and disruption, leading to serious illness."

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六微旨大... :	
帝曰：盛衰何如。歧伯曰：非其位則邪，當其位則正，邪則變甚，正則微。帝曰：何謂當位。歧伯曰：木運臨卯，火運臨午，土運臨四季，金運臨酉，水運臨子，所謂歲會，氣之平也。帝曰：非位何如。歧伯曰：歲不與會也。帝曰：土運之歲，上見太陰，火運之歲，上見少陽少陰，金運之歲，上見陽明，木運之歲，上見厥陰，水運之歲，上見太陽，柰何。歧伯曰：天之與會也。故《天元冊》曰：天符。天符歲會何如。歧伯曰：太一天符之會也。帝曰：其貴賤何如。歧伯曰：天符為執法，歲位為行令，太一天符為貴人。帝曰：邪之中也柰何。歧伯曰：中執法者，其病速而危。中行令者，其病徐而特。中貴人者，其病暴而死。帝曰：位之易也何如。歧伯曰：君位臣則順，臣位君則逆，逆則其病近，其害速，順則其病遠，其害微，所謂二火也。
		The Emperor asked: "What are these cycles of flourishing and decline like?" Qibo said: "When a qi is not in its proper position, it becomes pathogenic; when it is in its proper position, it remains normal. Pathogenic forces lead to marked changes, while normal conditions remain subtle." The Emperor asked: "What does 'proper position' mean?" Qibo said: "When the Wood phase governs Mao, Fire governs Wu, Earth governs the four seasons, Metal governs You, and Water governs Zi—this is called 'Suihui,' representing balanced qi." The Emperor asked: "What if it is not in the proper position?" Qibo said: "It does not align with Suihui." The Emperor asked: "In the year of Earth phase, Taiyin appears above; in the year of Fire phase, Shaoyang and Shaoyin appear above; in the year of Metal phase, Yangming appears above; in the year of Wood phase, Jueyin appears above; and in the year of Water phase, Taiyang appears above. Why is this so?" Qibo said: "This represents Heaven's alignment with Suihui." Therefore, the Tianyuan Ce says: "Heavenly Symbol." What is a year marked by both Heavenly Symbol and Suihui like? Qibo said: "It is the meeting of Taiyi's Heavenly Symbol." The Emperor asked: "What are their relative values?" Qibo said: "Heavenly Symbol is the enforcer of law; Suihui is the one who issues commands; Taiyi's Heavenly Symbol is a noble person." The Emperor asked: "What about cases where pathogenic influences strike?" Qibo said: "Those affected by the enforcer of law suffer sudden and dangerous illness." Those affected by the one issuing commands develop slow but distinctive illnesses. Those affected by the noble person suffer sudden illness and may die quickly. The Emperor asked: "What if their positions change?" Qibo said: "If the sovereign occupies a minister's position, it is harmonious; if the minister occupies the sovereign's position, it is disharmonious. Disharmonious conditions bring illness quickly and cause harm rapidly; harmonious conditions result in distant onset of illness and subtle harm. This is what is referred to as the two Fires."

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六微旨大... :	
帝曰：善。願聞其步何如。歧伯曰：所謂步者，六十度而有奇，故二十四步積盈百刻而成日也。帝曰：六氣應五行之變何如。歧伯曰：位有終始，氣有初中，上下不同，求之亦異也。帝曰：求之柰何。歧伯曰：天氣始於甲，地氣始於子，子甲相合，命曰歲立，謹候其時，氣可與期。
		The Emperor said: "Well said." I wish to hear about their steps. Qibo said: "What is meant by 'step' is that it covers sixty degrees and a little more. Therefore, twenty-four steps accumulate to fill one hundred moments, forming a day." The Emperor asked: "How do the six qi correspond to changes in the five elements?" Qibo said: "Positions have beginning and end; qi has initial and later stages. The upper and lower aspects differ, so their manifestations also vary." The Emperor asked: "How should one seek to understand this?" Qibo said: "Heavenly qi begins with Jia; earthly qi begins with Zi. When Jia and Zi combine, it is called the establishment of a year. Carefully observe its time, and the qi can be anticipated."

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六微旨大... :	
帝曰：願聞其歲六氣始終早晏何如。歧伯曰：明乎哉問也。甲子之歲，初之氣天數始於水下一刻，終於八十七刻半。二之氣始於八十七刻六分，終於七十五刻。三之氣始於七十六刻，終於六十二刻半。四之氣始於六十二刻六分，終於五十刻。五之氣始於五十一刻，終於三十七刻半。六之氣始於三十七刻六分，終於二十五刻。所謂初六，天之數也。乙丑歲初之氣天數始於二十六刻，終於一十二刻半。二之氣始於一十二刻六分，終於水下百刻。三之氣始於一刻，終於八十七刻半。四之氣始於八十七刻六分，終於七十五刻。五之氣始於七十六刻，終於六十二刻半。六之氣始於六十二刻六分，終於五十刻。所謂六二，天之數也。丙寅歲，初之氣天數始於五十一刻，終於三十七刻半。二之氣始於三十七刻六分，終於二十五刻。三之氣始於二十六刻，終於一十二刻半。四之氣始於一十二刻六分，終於水下百刻。五之氣始於一刻，終於八十七刻半。六之氣始於八十七刻六分，終於七十五刻。所謂六三，天之數也。丁卯歲，初之氣天數始於七十六刻，終於六十二刻半。二之氣始於六十二刻六分，終於五十刻。三之氣始於五十一刻，終於三十七刻半。四之氣始於三十七刻六分，終於二十五刻。五之氣始於二十六刻，終於一十二刻半。六之氣始於一十二刻六分，終於水下百刻。所謂六四，天之數也。次戊辰歲，初之氣復始於一刻，常如是無已，周而復始。帝曰：願聞其歲候何如。歧伯曰：悉乎哉問也。日行一周，天氣始於一刻，日行再周，天氣始於二十六刻，日行三周，天氣始於五十一刻，日行四周，天氣始於七十六刻，日行五周，天氣復始於一刻，所謂一紀也。是故寅午戌歲氣會同，卯未亥歲氣會同，辰申子歲氣會同，覽巳酉丑歲氣會同，終而復始。
		The Emperor asked: "I wish to hear about how the six qi of each year begin and end early or late." Qibo said: "How clear is your question!" In the year Jiazi, the first qi begins at one moment below Water and ends at eighty-seven and a half moments. The second qi begins at eighty-seven and six-tenths moments, ending at seventy-five moments. The third qi begins at seventy-six moments, ending at sixty-two and a half moments. The fourth qi begins at sixty-two and six-tenths moments, and ends at fifty moments. The fifth qi begins at fifty-one moments, ending at thirty-seven and a half moments. The sixth qi begins at thirty-seven and six-tenths moments and ends at twenty-five moments. This is what is referred to as "the first through the sixth"—the numbers of Heaven. In the year Yichou, the first qi of Heavenly numbers begins at twenty-six moments and ends at twelve and a half moments. The second qi begins at twelve and six-tenths moments, concluding at one hundred moments below Water. The third qi begins at one moment and ends at eighty-seven and a-half moments. The fourth qi begins at eighty-seven and six tenths moments, ending at seventy-five momentos. The fifth qi begins at seventy-six momentos and ends at sixty-two and a-half momentos. The sixth qi begins at sixty-two and six tenths momentos, ending at fifty momentos. This is what is known as "six two"—the numbers of Heaven. In the year Bingyin, the first qi Heavenly number begins at fifty-one momentos and ends at thirty-seven and a-half momentos. The second qi begins at thirty-seven and six tenths momentos and ends at twenty-five momentos. The third qi begins at twenty-six momentos, ending at twelve and a-half momentos. The fourth qi begins at twelve and six tenths momentos, concluding at one hundred momentos below Water. The fifth qi begins at one momento and ends at eighty-seven and half momentos. The sixth qi begins at eighty-seven and six tenth momentos, ending at seventy-five momentos. This is what is known as "six three"—the numbers of Heaven. In the year Dingmao, the first qi of Heaven's number begins at seventy-six moments and ends at sixty-two and one-half moments. The second qi begins at sixty-two and six tenth moments, ending at fifty moments. The third qi begins at fifty-one moments and ends at thirty-seven and one-half moments. The fourth qi begins at thirty-seven and six tenth moments and ends at twenty-five moment. The fifth qi begins at twenty-six moments and concludes at twelve and one-half moment. The sixth qi begins at twelve and six-tenths moments and ends at one hundred moments below Water. What is referred to as "six four" is the number of Heaven. In the subsequent year Wuchen, the first qi begins again at one moment, as it always is without end, cycling and repeating endlessly. The Emperor said: "I would like to hear how the annual signs manifest." Qibo replied: "What a thorough question this is." When the sun completes one full cycle, Heaven's qi begins at one moment. When the sun completes two cycles, Heaven's qi begins at twenty-six moments. When it completes three cycles, Heaven's qi starts at fifty-one moments. After four cycles, Heaven's qi commences at seventy-six moments. Upon completing five cycles, Heaven's qi returns to begin once again at one moment—this is what is called "one ji." Therefore, the years of Yin, Wu, and Xu have their qi united; the years of Mao, Wei, and Hai also have their qi united. The years of Chen, Shen, and Zi share a unified qi as well, while the years of Si, You, and Chou likewise have their qi in harmony—this cycle concludes and begins anew repeatedly.

8
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六微旨大... :	
帝曰：願聞其用也。歧伯曰：言天者求之本，言地者求之位，言人者求之氣交。帝曰：何謂氣交。歧伯曰：上下之位，氣交之中，人之居也。故曰：天樞之上，天氣主之，天樞之下，地氣主之，氣交之分，人氣從之，萬物由之，此之謂也。
		The Emperor said: "I would like to hear about its application." Qibo replied: "To speak of Heaven, one must seek the root; to speak of Earth, one must seek position; and to speak of humanity, one must seek where qi intermingles." The Emperor asked: "What is meant by 'qi intermingling'?" Qibo replied: "Between the positions of above and below, in the midst of where qi intermingles, is where humans dwell." Therefore, it is said: "Above the celestial axis, Heaven's qi governs; below the celestial axis, Earth's qi governs. In the region where qi intermingles, human qi follows accordingly, and all things originate from this—this is what it means."

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六微旨大... :	
帝曰：何謂初中。歧伯曰：初凡三十度而有奇，中氣同法。帝曰：初中何也。歧伯曰：所以分天地也。帝曰：願卒聞之。歧伯曰：初者，地氣也，中者，天氣也。帝曰：其升降何如。歧伯曰：氣之升降，天地之更用也。帝曰：願聞其用何如。歧伯曰：升已而降，降者謂天。降已而升，升者謂地。天氣下降，氣流于地，地氣上升，氣騰于天，故高下相召，升降相因，而變作矣。
		The Emperor asked: "What is meant by 'initial' and 'middle'?" Qibo replied: "The initial phase encompasses thirty degrees with a remainder, and the middle qi follows the same principle." The Emperor asked: "What are 'initial' and 'middle', then?" Qibo replied: "They serve to distinguish Heaven from Earth." The Emperor said: "I would like to hear the full explanation." Qibo replied: "The initial is Earth's qi, and the middle is Heaven's qi." The Emperor asked: "How do they rise and fall?" Qibo replied: "The rising and falling of qi is the mutual operation of Heaven and Earth." The Emperor said: "I would like to hear what their operations are like." Qibo replied: "After rising, it descends; the descending is called Heaven's action." After descending, it rises again; the rising is referred to as Earth's action. When Heaven's qi descends, it flows into Earth; when Earth's qi ascends, it rises toward Heaven. Thus, the high and low call to each other, and rising and falling depend on one another, giving rise to transformation.

10
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六微旨大... :	
帝曰：善。寒濕遘溝，燥熱相臨，風火相值，其有聞乎。歧伯曰：氣有勝復，勝復之作，有德有化，有用有變，變則邪氣居之。帝曰：何謂邪乎。歧伯曰：夫物之生從於化，物之極由乎變，變化之相薄，成敗之所由也。故氣有往復，用有遲速，四者之有而化而變，風之來也。帝曰：遲速往復，風所由生，而化而變，故因盛衰之變耳。成敗倚伏遊乎中，何也。歧伯曰：成敗倚伏生乎動，動而不已，則變作矣。帝曰：有期乎。歧伯曰：不生不化，靜之期也。帝曰：不生化乎。歧伯曰：出入廢則神機化滅，升降息則氣立孤危。故非出入，則無以生長壯老已，非升降，則無以生長化收藏。是以升降出入，無器不有。故器者生化之宇，器散則分之，生化息矣。故無不出入，無木升降。化有小大，期有近遠，四者之有而貴常守，反常則災害至矣。故曰無形無患，此之謂也。帝曰：善。有不生不化乎。歧伯曰：悉乎哉問也。與道合同，惟真人也。帝曰：善。
		The Emperor said: "Well said." Cold and dampness converge in the channels, dryness and heat approach each other, wind and fire meet—have you heard of such phenomena? Qibo replied: "Qi has dominance and restoration. When there is dominance and restoration, there are virtue, transformation, utility, and change; when there is change, pathogenic qi will take hold." The Emperor asked: "What is meant by 'pathogenic qi'?" Qibo replied: "The birth of things follows transformation, and the culmination of things arises from change. The mutual interaction between transformation and change is where success and failure originate." Therefore, qi has ebb and flow, its operation varies in speed; with these four factors present, transformation and change occur—this is how wind arises. The Emperor said: "The slow or rapid ebb and flow of qi are the causes for wind to arise, leading to transformation and change. Thus, it is merely due to variations in prosperity and decline." Success and failure alternate and move within the midst—why is this so? Qibo replied: "The interplay of success and failure arises from movement. When movement does not cease, transformation occurs." The Emperor asked: "Is there a fixed time for this?" Qibo replied: "When there is no generation and transformation, it is the period of stillness." The Emperor asked: "Is there a time when generation and transformation do not occur?" Qibo replied: "When entry and exit cease, the divine mechanism of transformation perishes; when rising and falling stop, qi becomes isolated and perilous." Therefore, without entry and exit, there would be no process of birth, growth, maturity, aging, or death; without rising and falling, there would be no generation, transformation, or storage. Therefore, the processes of rising, falling, entering, and exiting are present in all things without exception. Therefore, an object is the domain where generation and transformation occur; when the object disperses, it separates, and generation and transformation cease. Thus, there is no object without entry and exit, no thing without rising and falling. Transformation varies in degree—some small, some great; the time of occurrence also differs—some near, some distant. With these four factors present, it is most important to maintain constancy. When normalcy is reversed, calamities will arise. Therefore, it is said: "When there is no abnormal form, there is no affliction"—this is what that means. The Emperor said: "Well said." Is there anything without generation and transformation? Qibo replied: "What a thorough question this is." To be in harmony with the Dao is to be a true person. The Emperor said: "Well said." 

69. 氣交變大論 Treatise on the Great Transformations of Qi Exchange

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	氣交變大... :	
黃帝問曰：五運更治，上應天朞，陰陽往復，寒暑迎隨，真邪相薄，內外分離，六經波蕩，五氣傾移，太過不及，專勝兼并，願言其始，而有常名，可得聞乎。歧伯稽首再拜對曰：昭乎哉問也，是明道也。此上帝所貴，先師傳之，臣雖不敏，往聞其旨。帝曰：余聞得其人不教，是謂失道，傳非其人，慢泄天寶。余誠菲德，未足以受至道，然而眾子哀其不終，願夫子保於無窮，流於無極，余司其事則而行之，柰何。歧伯曰：請遂言之也。上經曰：夫道者，上知天文，下知地理，中知人事，可以長久，此之謂也。帝曰：何謂也。歧伯曰：本氣，位也。位天者，天文也。位地者，地理也。通於人氣之變化者，人事也，故太過者先天，不及者後天，所謂治化而人應之也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "The Five Periods of Movement take turns governing, corresponding to the celestial cycle above. Yin and Yang alternate in their movements; cold and heat follow each other. True qi and pathogenic factors clash, separating internally from externally. The Six Meridians are agitated, the Five Qi shift positions. When one is excessive or insufficient, it dominates or combines with others. I wish to hear of its beginning, and if there is a constant name for this, may I be informed?" Qibo bowed respectfully twice in succession and replied: "What an enlightened question! This clarifies the Way." This is what the Supreme Deity values; previous teachers have transmitted it. Though I am not very wise, I have previously heard its meaning. The Emperor said: "I have heard that if one finds a suitable person but does not teach him, it is called losing the Way. Transmitting to an unsuitable person is a disrespectful and reckless exposure of heavenly treasures." "I am truly of humble virtue and insufficient to receive the highest Way. However, many disciples pity its failure to be completed; I wish Master would preserve it eternally, transmitting it without end. I shall oversee this matter and follow accordingly—how should we proceed?" Qibo said: "Please allow me to explain in full." The Upper Classic says: "The Way is such that one knows the celestial phenomena above, understands geography below, and comprehends human affairs in between. With this knowledge, one can endure for a long time; this is what it means." The Emperor said: "What does this mean?" Qibo said: "The fundamental qi refers to position." "To be in the position of Heaven" means celestial phenomena. "To be in the position of Earth" means geography. Those who understand the changes and transformations in human qi comprehend human affairs. Therefore, when one is excessive, it precedes Heaven; when insufficient, it follows Earth—this is what is meant by regulation and transformation with humans responding accordingly.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	氣交變大... :	
帝曰：五運之化，太過何如。歧伯曰：歲木太過，風氣流行，脾土受邪。民病飱泄食減，體重煩冤，腸鳴腹支滿，上應歲星。甚則忽忽善怒，眩冒巔疾，化氣不政，生氣獨治，雲物飛動，草木不寧，甚而搖落，反脇痛而吐甚，衝陽絕者，死不治，上應太白星。歲火太過，炎暑流行，金肺受邪。民病瘧，少氣欬喘，血溢血泄注下，嗌燥耳聾，中熱肩背熱，上應熒惑星。甚則胸中痛脇支滿脇痛，膺背肩胛間痛，兩臂內痛，身熱骨痛，而為浸淫。收氣不行，長氣獨明，雨水霜寒，上應辰星。上臨少陰少陽，火燔焫，冰泉涸，物焦槁，病反譫妄狂越，欬喘息鳴，下甚血溢泄不已，太淵絕者，死不治，上應熒惑星。歲土太過，雨濕流行，腎水受邪。民病腹痛，清厥意不樂，體重煩冤，上應鎮星。甚則肌肉萎，足痿不收，行善瘈，腳下痛，飲發中滿食減，四支不舉。變生得位，藏氣伏，化氣獨治之，泉涌河衍，涸澤生魚，風雨大至，土崩潰，鱗見于陸，病腹滿溏泄腸鳴，反下甚而太谿絕者，死不治，上應歲星。歲金太過，燥氣流行，肝木受邪。民病兩脇下少腹痛，目赤痛眥瘍耳無所聞。肅殺而甚，則體重煩冤，胸痛引背，兩脇滿且痛引少腹，上應太白星。甚則喘欬逆氣，肩背痛，尻陰股膝髀腨䯒足皆病，上應熒惑星。收氣峻，生氣下，草木斂，蒼乾凋隕，病反暴痛，胠脇不可反側，欬逆甚而血溢，太衝絕者，死不治，上應太白星。歲水太過，寒氣流行，邪害心火。民病身熱煩心躁悸，陰厥上下中寒，譫妄心痛，寒氣早至，上應辰星。甚則腹大脛腫，喘欬寖汗出憎風，大雨至，埃霧朦鬱，上應鎮星。上臨太陽，雨冰雪霜不時降，濕氣變物，病反腹滿腸鳴溏泄，食不化，渴而妄冒，神門絕者，死不治，上應熒惑辰星。
		The Emperor said: "Regarding the transformations of the Five Periods, what happens when one is excessive?" Qibo said: "In a year with excessive Wood, wind qi prevails and spreads; the spleen Earth suffers from pathogenic influences." The people suffer from diarrhea, reduced appetite, heavy body and irritability, intestinal rumbling, abdominal distension and fullness; this corresponds above to the planet Jupiter. In severe cases, people become easily agitated and angry, experience dizziness and vertigo, and suffer from headaches. The transforming qi fails to function properly, while the generating qi dominates alone. Clouds and objects move restlessly; grasses and trees are unsettled, shaking and falling prematurely. People may also suffer from lateral chest pain and severe vomiting. If the Chongyang pulse is cut off, death occurs without cure; this corresponds above to the star Tai Bai (Venus). In a year with excessive Fire, intense heat prevails; Metal and the lungs suffer from pathogenic influences. The people suffer from malaria, shortness of breath, coughing and wheezing, blood vomiting or bleeding from the bowels, dry throat, deafness, internal heat, and fever in the shoulders and back; this corresponds above to Mars. In severe cases, there is chest pain and lateral abdominal distension with pain in the flanks; pain appears in the upper back, shoulders, and scapular regions, as well as inner arm pain. The body feels hot, bones ache, and sores spread gradually. The gathering qi fails to function, while the growing qi shines alone; rains, frost, and cold prevail above, corresponding to the planet Venus. When the Upper Heaven governs Shaoyin and Shaoyang, fire burns fiercely; icy springs dry up, things wither and become scorched. Diseases manifest as delirium, madness, coughing, wheezing, and noisy breathing. In severe cases, blood continues to flow from vomiting or diarrhea. If the Taiyuan pulse is cut off, death results without cure; this corresponds above to Mars (the planet of confusion). In a year with excessive Earth, rain and dampness prevail; the kidneys and Water suffer from pathogenic influences. The people suffer abdominal pain, cold extremities, low spirits, a heavy body, and irritability; this corresponds above to Saturn. In severe cases, the muscles and flesh atrophy; the legs become weak and unable to move properly. People are prone to convulsions, suffer pain in their feet, experience fluid retention with abdominal distension and reduced appetite, and find it difficult to raise their limbs. When transformation occurs in its proper position, the stored qi becomes latent; the transforming qi governs alone. Springs surge and rivers overflow, dry ponds give birth to fish, heavy rains and winds arrive suddenly, earth collapses, and scaled creatures appear on land. Diseases manifest as abdominal distension, loose stools, intestinal rumbling; if diarrhea worsens severely and the Taixi pulse is cut off, death follows without cure; this corresponds above to Jupiter (the planet of years). In a year with excessive Metal, dryness prevails; the liver and Wood suffer from pathogenic influences. The people suffer pain in both flanks and lower abdomen, red eyes with painful swelling of the eyelids, and deafness. If dry 肃杀 (harsh killing) is severe, the body feels heavy and irritable; chest pain radiates to the back, with fullness and pain in both flanks extending to the lower abdomen; this corresponds above to Venus. In severe cases, coughing and wheezing with reversed qi occur; pain appears in the shoulders and back, as well as in the hips, genital area, thighs, knees, calves, shins, and feet—all are affected; this corresponds above to Mars. The gathering qi becomes severe, while the generating qi descends; grasses and trees contract, turn dry, and wither. Diseases manifest as sudden pain, inability to roll or lie on one's side due to lateral flank pain, severe coughing with upward rushing qi and blood vomiting. If the Taichong pulse is cut off, death ensues without cure; this corresponds above to Venus (the planet of metal). In a year with excessive Water, cold qi prevails; pathogenic influences harm the Heart and Fire. The people suffer from body heat, irritability, restlessness, palpitations, cold in the lower extremities with internal cold affecting upper and middle regions, delirium, heart pain; cold qi arrives early; this corresponds above to Mercury. In severe cases, abdominal distension and swollen shins occur; coughing with profuse sweating and aversion to wind appear. Heavy rains arrive, dust and fog become dense and oppressive; this corresponds above to Saturn (the planet of Earth). When the Upper Heaven governs Taiyang, rain, snow, ice, and frost fall at irregular times; dampness transforms objects. Diseases manifest as abdominal fullness, intestinal rumbling with loose stools, indigestion, thirst accompanied by delirium. If the Shenmen pulse is cut off, death without cure follows; this corresponds above to both Mars (the planet of chaos) and Mercury.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	氣交變大... :	
帝曰：善。其不及，何如。歧伯曰：悉乎哉問也。歲木不及，燥迺大行，生氣失應，草木晚榮，肅殺而甚，則剛木辟著，悉萎蒼乾，上應太白星，民病中清，胠脇痛少腹痛，腸鳴溏泄，涼雨時至，上應太白星，其穀蒼。上臨陽明，生氣失政，草木再榮，化氣迺急，上應太白鎮星，其主蒼早。復則炎暑流火，濕性燥，柔脆草木焦槁，下體再生，華實齊化，病寒熱瘡瘍疿胗癰痤，上應熒惑太白，其穀白堅。白露早降，收殺氣行，寒雨害物，蟲食甘黃，脾土受邪，赤氣後化，心氣晚治，上勝肺金，白氣迺屈，其穀不成，欬而鼽，上應熒惑太白星。歲火不及，寒乃大行，長政不用，物榮而下凝，慘而甚則陽氣不化，迺折榮美，上應辰星，民病胸中痛脇支滿兩脇痛，膺背肩胛間及兩臂內痛，鬱冒朦昧，心痛暴瘖，胸腹大脇，下與腰背相引而痛，甚則屈不能伸，髖髀如別，上應熒惑辰星，其穀丹。復則埃鬱大雨且至，黑氣迺辱，病鶩溏腹滿，食飲不下，寒中腸鳴，泄注腹痛，暴攣痿痺，足不任身，上應鎮星辰星，玄穀不成。歲土不及，風迺大行，化氣不令，草木茂榮，飄揚而甚，秀而不實，上應歲星，民病飱泄霍亂，體重腹痛，筋骨繇復，肌肉瞤酸，善怒，歲氣舉事，蟄蟲早附，咸病寒中，上應歲星鎮星，其穀黅。復則收政嚴峻，名木蒼凋，胸脇暴痛，下引少腹善大息，蟲食甘黃，氣客於脾，黅穀迺減，民食少失味，蒼穀迺損，上應太白歲星。上臨厥陰，流水不冰，蟄蟲來見，藏氣不用，白迺不復，上應歲星，民迺康。歲金不及，炎火迺行，生氣迺用，長氣專勝，庶物以茂，燥爍以行，上應熒惑星，民病肩背瞀重，鼽嚏血便注下，收氣迺後，上應太白星，其穀堅芒。復則寒雨暴至，迺零冰雹霜雪殺物，陰厥且格陽，反上行頭腦戶痛，延及囟頂發熱，上應辰星，丹穀不成，民病口瘡，甚則心痛。歲水不及，濕迺大行，長氣反用，其化迺速，暑雨數至，上應鎮星，民病腹滿身重，濡泄寒瘍流水，腰股痛發，膕腨股膝不便，煩冤足痿清厥，腳下痛，甚則跗腫，藏氣不政，腎氣不衡，上應辰星，其穀秬。上臨太陰，則大寒數舉，蟄蟲早藏，地積堅冰，陽光不治，民病寒疾於下，甚則腹滿浮腫，上應鎮星，其主黅穀。復則大風暴發，草偃木零，生長不鮮，面色時變，筋骨併辟，肉膶瘛，目視䀮䀮，物疎璺，肌肉胗發，氣并鬲中，痛於心腹，黃氣迺損，其穀不登，上應歲星。
		The Emperor said: "Good." What about when they are insufficient? Qibo said: "How thorough your question is!" In a year of insufficient Wood, dryness becomes rampant; the generating qi fails to respond. Grasses and trees bloom late. If 肃杀 (harsh destruction) is severe, then strong woods crack open and become prominent, all withering into dryness and grayness; this corresponds above to Venus, and people suffer from internal cold, lateral flank pain, lower abdominal pain, intestinal rumbling, and loose stools. Cool rains arrive at times; this also corresponds above to Venus; the grain of that year is green (cang). When the Upper Heaven governs Yangming, the generating qi fails in its function; grasses and trees bloom twice. The transforming qi becomes urgent; this corresponds above to Venus and Saturn; the grain is green but ripens early. When the correcting influence occurs, intense heat and fire spread; dampness becomes dry. Delicate and brittle grasses and trees wither and die prematurely; lower parts of plants regenerate, flowers and fruits mature simultaneously. Diseases manifest as chills, fever, sores, swellings, abscesses, and boils; this corresponds above to Mars and Venus; the grain is white and firm. White Dew arrives early, and the gathering-killing qi prevails. Cold rains harm objects; insects consume sweet yellow grains. The spleen Earth suffers pathogenic influences. Red qi lags in transformation, while Heart qi is delayed in regulation. Above, it overcomes Lung Metal, causing white qi to be suppressed. Crops fail to mature; coughing and nasal discharge occur; this corresponds above to Mars and the planet of Venus. In a year of insufficient Fire, cold prevails greatly; the function of growth fails. Objects flourish but remain condensed below. If this becomes severe, Yang qi cannot transform, and beautiful blossoms are broken off prematurely; this corresponds above to Mercury (the planet of Water). The people suffer from chest pain, lateral abdominal distension, and pain in both flanks; discomfort appears between the upper back, shoulders, scapulae, and inner arms. Dizziness and confusion occur, along with heart pain, sudden aphonia, and a large, distended chest and abdomen extending to the sides. Pain radiates downward into the waist and back, so severe that one cannot straighten the body; hips and thighs feel as if separated—this corresponds above to Mars and Mercury (the planets of Fire and Water); the grain is red (dan). When the correcting influence occurs, dust becomes dense and heavy rains arrive. Black qi prevails; diseases manifest as loose stools with mucus, abdominal distension, inability to swallow food or drink, internal cold with intestinal rumbling, diarrhea with abdominal pain, sudden cramps, atrophy, numbness, and legs unable to bear the body's weight—this corresponds above to Saturn and Mercury (the stars of Earth and Water); dark-colored grains fail to mature. In a year of insufficient Earth, wind prevails greatly; the transforming qi fails to govern properly. Grasses and trees flourish but are blown about severely, blooming without bearing fruit—this corresponds above to Jupiter (the star of Wood). The people suffer from diarrhea with vomiting, heavy body, abdominal pain, trembling muscles and bones, twitching and aching flesh, irritability; when the annual qi is active in affairs, hibernating insects emerge early. Many suffer from cold in the middle jiao—this corresponds above to Jupiter and Saturn (the stars of Wood and Earth); the grain is yellow (shen). When the correcting influence occurs, the gathering function becomes severe; tall trees turn gray and wither. Sudden chest and flank pain appears, radiating downward to the lower abdomen with frequent sighing. Insects consume sweet yellow grains; pathogenic qi lodges in the spleen. Yellow grain decreases; people eat less and lose taste for food; green grain is damaged—this corresponds above to Venus and Jupiter (the stars of Metal and Wood). When the Upper Heaven governs Jueyin, flowing water does not freeze; hibernating insects appear early. The storing qi fails to function; white (Metal) is not restored—this corresponds above to Jupiter; the people then enjoy health. In a year of insufficient Metal, fire prevails; the generating qi becomes active while the growing qi dominates. Objects flourish abundantly, and dryness spreads—this corresponds above to Mars (the star of Fire). The people suffer from dizziness, heaviness in the shoulders and back, nasal discharge, sneezing, bloody stools or diarrhea. When the gathering qi lags behind, this corresponds above to Venus; the grains have firm, sharp husks. When the correcting influence occurs, cold rains arrive suddenly; hail and frost or snow destroy objects. Yin cold blocks Yang, which rebels upward to cause pain in the head, brain, and forehead, extending to the fontanelle with fever—this corresponds above to Mercury (the star of Water). Red grains fail to mature; people suffer from mouth sores, and severe cases develop heart pain. In a year of insufficient Water, dampness prevails greatly; the growing qi acts in reverse, and transformation occurs rapidly. Hot rains arrive frequently—this corresponds above to Saturn (the star of Earth). The people suffer from abdominal distension, heavy body, loose stools with cold sores oozing fluid, pain in the waist and thighs, difficulty moving the knees and calves, irritability, foot atrophy with cold extremities, and foot pain. In severe cases, swelling appears on the ankles; the storing qi fails to function properly; Kidney qi becomes unbalanced—this corresponds above to Mercury; the grain is black (ju). When the Upper Heaven governs Taixuan, severe cold occurs frequently; hibernating insects retreat early. The earth accumulates thick ice; Yang warmth fails to regulate properly. People suffer from lower-body cold diseases; in severe cases, abdominal distension and swelling occur—this corresponds above to Saturn; the grain is yellow (sheng gu). When the correcting influence occurs, violent winds suddenly arise; grasses fall and trees shed their leaves. Growth is not vigorous; facial complexion changes frequently. Muscles and bones crack open; flesh twitches convulsively; eyes appear dim and blurred; objects become loose and cracked. Skin eruptions occur; qi accumulates in the chest, causing pain in the heart and abdomen. Yellow qi is damaged; grain fails to be harvested—this corresponds above to Jupiter.
