顯示相似段落
	氣交變大... :	
帝曰：善。願聞其時也。歧伯曰：悉哉問也。木不及，春有鳴條律暢之化，則秋有霧露清涼之政。春有慘凄殘賊之勝，則夏有炎暑燔爍之復。其眚東，其藏肝，其病內舍胠脇，外在關節。火不及，夏有炳明光顯之化，則冬有嚴肅霜寒之政。夏有慘淒凝冽之勝，則不時有埃昏大雨之復。其眚南，其藏心，其病內舍膺脇，外在經絡。土不及，四維有埃雲潤澤之化，則春有鳴條鼓拆之政。四維發振拉飄騰之變，則秋有肅殺霖霪之復。其眚四維，其藏脾，其病內舍心腹，外在肌肉四支。金不及，夏有光顯鬱蒸之令，則冬有嚴凝整肅之應。夏有炎爍燔燎之變，則秋有冰雹霜雪之復。其眚西，其藏肺，其病內舍膺脇肩背，外在皮毛。水不及，四維有湍潤埃雲之化，則不時有和風生發之應。四維發埃昏驟注之變，則不時有飄蕩振拉之復。其眚北，其藏腎，其病內舍腰脊骨髓，外在谿谷踹膝。夫五運之政，猶權衡也，高者抑之，下者舉之，化者應之，變者復之，此生長化成收藏之理，氣之常也，失常則天地四塞矣。故曰：天地之動靜，神明為之紀，陰陽之往復，寒暑彰其兆，此之謂也。
		The Emperor said: "Good." I wish to hear about their seasons. Qibo said: "How thorough your question is!" When Wood is insufficient, in spring there will be the transformation of rustling branches and harmonious laws; thus, in autumn there will be governance by mist, dew, coolness, and clarity. If spring experiences harsh, destructive influences, then summer will see a corrective response of intense heat and burning dryness. Its disaster occurs in the east; its storage is in the liver; its diseases internally reside in the flanks, externally manifesting in the joints. When Fire is insufficient, summer will experience bright and luminous transformation; thus, winter will see governance by severity, frost, and cold. If summer experiences harsh cold and chilling influences, then at irregular times there will be a corrective response of dust-laden skies and heavy rains. Its disaster occurs in the south; its storage is in the heart; its diseases internally reside between the chest and flanks, externally manifesting along meridians and collaterals. When Earth is insufficient, the four directions will experience transformation by dusty clouds and moistening; thus, spring will see governance through rustling branches and cracking sounds. If the four directions experience shaking, pulling, and drifting changes, then in autumn there will be a corrective response through severe killing and continuous drizzle. Its disaster occurs at the four directions; its storage is in the spleen; its diseases internally affect the heart and abdomen, externally manifesting as muscle and limb disorders. When Metal is insufficient, summer experiences bright and oppressive heat; thus, winter sees a response of severe cold and order. If summer brings intense burning and fire-like changes, then autumn will see corrective responses in the form of hail, frost, and snow. Its disaster occurs to the west; its storage is in the lungs; its diseases internally affect between the chest, flanks, shoulders, and back, externally manifesting on the skin and hair. When Water is insufficient, transformation in the four directions brings turbulent moisture and dusty clouds; thus, at irregular times there will be responses of harmonious winds promoting growth. If the four directions experience sudden changes with dusty skies and heavy rain, then at irregular times corrective responses will manifest as drifting, shaking, and pulling. Its disaster occurs to the north; its storage is in the kidneys; its diseases internally affect the waist, spine, bones, and marrow, externally manifesting at the sinews, valleys, calves, and knees. The governance of the Five Periods is like a balance and scale: elevate what is low, suppress what is high; respond to transformation, correct for change. This is the principle of generation, growth, transformation, maturation, and storage—the normal functioning of qi. When this norm is lost, Heaven and Earth become blocked in all directions. Therefore it is said: "The movements and stillness of Heaven and Earth are recorded by the Divine; the alternation of Yin and Yang, with cold and heat revealing their omens—this is what it means."

5
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	氣交變大... :	
帝曰：夫子之言五氣之變，四時之應，可謂悉矣。夫氣之動亂，觸遇而作，發無常會，卒然災合，何以期之。歧伯曰：夫氣之動變，固不常在，而德化政令災變不同其候也。帝曰：何謂也。歧伯曰：東方生風，風生木，其德敷和，其化生榮，其政舒啟，其令風，其變振發，其災散落。南方生熱，熱生火，其德彰顯，其化蕃茂，其政明曜，其令熱，其變銷爍，其災燔焫。中央生濕，濕生土，其德溽蒸，其化豈備，其政安靜，其令濕，其變驟注，其災霖潰。西方生燥，燥生金，其德清潔，其化緊斂，其政勁切，其令燥，其變肅殺，其災蒼隕。北方生寒，寒生水，其德淒滄，其化清謐，其政凝肅，其令寒，其變凓冽，其災冰雪霜雹。是以察其動也，有德有化，有政有令，有變有災，而物由之，而人應之也。
		The Emperor said: "Your explanation of the transformations of the Five Qi and their responses in the Four Seasons is truly thorough." When qi becomes disturbed and chaotic, it arises upon contact with external influences. Its onset is unpredictable; sudden disasters occur—how can one anticipate them?" Qibo said: "The movement and transformation of qi are indeed not constant; the signs of virtue, transformation, governance, commands, disasters, and changes differ accordingly." The Emperor said: "What do you mean?" Qibo said: "In the East, birth gives rise to wind; wind generates Wood. Its virtue is spreading harmony; its transformation brings growth and flourishing; its governance promotes relaxation and opening; its command is wind; its change manifests as shaking and bursting forth; its disaster results in scattering and falling." In the South, birth gives rise to heat; heat generates Fire. Its virtue is clear manifestation; its transformation brings abundance and flourishing growth; its governance is bright and shining; its command is heat; its change manifests as burning and scorching; its disaster results in fire and destruction. In the Center, birth gives rise to dampness; dampness generates Earth. Its virtue is moist and steaming; its transformation brings full preparation; its governance is calm and stable; its command is dampness; its change manifests as sudden heavy rain; its disaster results in continuous downpours and collapse. In the West, birth gives rise to dryness; dryness generates Metal. Its virtue is clarity and purity; its transformation brings tightening and contraction; its governance is firm and sharp; its command is dryness; its change manifests as severe killing; its disaster results in withering and falling. In the North, birth gives rise to cold; cold generates Water. Its virtue is bleak and desolate; its transformation brings clarity and stillness; its governance is solid and severe; its command is cold; its change manifests as freezing and piercing chill; its disaster results in ice, snow, frost, and hail. Therefore, by observing its movements, one recognizes virtue, transformation, governance, command, change, and disaster. Objects respond accordingly, and humans follow in kind.

6
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	氣交變大... :	
帝曰：夫子之言歲候，不及其太過，而上應五星。今夫德化政令災眚變易，非常而有也，卒然而動，其亦為之變乎。歧伯曰：承天而行之，故無妄動，無不應也。卒然而動者，氣之交變也，其不應焉。故曰應常不應卒，此之謂也。帝曰：其應柰何。歧伯曰：各從其氣化也。帝曰：其行之徐疾逆順何如。歧伯曰：以道留久，逆守而小，是謂省下。以道而去，去而速來，曲而過之，是謂省遺過也。久留而環，或離或附，是謂議災與其德也。應近則小，應遠則大。芒而大倍常之一其化甚，大常之二其眚即也。小常之一其化減，小常之二是謂臨視，省下之過與其德也。德者福之，過者伐之。是以象之見也，高而遠則小，下而近則大，故大則喜怒邇，小則禍福遠。歲運太過，則運星北越，運氣相得，則各行以道。故歲運太過，畏星失色而兼其母，不及，則色兼其所不勝。肖者瞿瞿，莫知其妙，閔閔之當，孰者為良，妄行無徵，示畏侯王。帝曰：其災應何如。歧伯曰：亦各從其化也，故時至有盛衰，凌犯有逆順，留守有多少，形見有善惡，宿屬有勝負，徵應有吉凶矣。帝曰：其善惡，何謂也。歧伯曰：有喜有怒，有憂有喪，有澤有燥，此象之常也，必謹察之。帝曰：六者，高下異乎。歧伯曰：象見高下其應一也，故人亦應之。
		The Emperor said: "Your explanation of the annual signs includes both insufficiency and excess, corresponding above to the Five Stars." Now, these virtues, transformations, governance, commands, calamities, disasters, changes, and variations are not constant but sudden in their occurrence. Do they also bring about change?" Qibo said: "Following Heaven's will, there is no arbitrary movement; nothing remains unresponsive." Sudden movements are the result of qi changing abruptly; in such cases, responses may not occur. Therefore it is said: "Responses follow regular patterns but do not necessarily respond to sudden changes"—this is what it means. The Emperor asked: "What are these responses like?" Qibo said: "They follow the transformation of their respective qi." The Emperor asked: "What are the differences in speed, slowness, reversal, and harmony when they manifest?" Qibo said: "When following the Way, it lingers for a long time. When opposing forces are slight and held back, this is called restraint at lower levels." "When following the Way but departing from it, leaving quickly yet returning swiftly, and passing by in a curved path—this is called overlooking past excesses." "When lingering for a long time and forming circles, sometimes separating and sometimes attaching—it is said to be deliberating over disasters and virtue." When the response is near, it is minor; when distant, it becomes great. If its brilliance and magnitude double that of normal, its transformation is severe; if it triples the norm, then disaster immediately follows. If it is one part less than normal, its transformation diminishes; if two parts less, this is called a visitation for inspection—reviewing past excesses and virtue. Virtue brings blessings; transgressions invite punishment. Therefore, when the signs appear: if high and distant, they are small; if low and near, they are great. Hence, when great, joys and angers are immediate; when small, calamities and blessings remain far off. When the annual movement is excessive, the corresponding star moves northward beyond its position; when the movement and qi harmonize properly, they each follow their respective paths in accordance with the Way. Therefore, when an annual movement is excessive, its corresponding star loses color and combines with its mother element; when it is insufficient, the color merges with what it cannot overcome. Those who mimic may appear confused, none understanding its subtleties; those who act with concern, who among them is truly capable? Acting recklessly without evidence shows disregard for the Son of Heaven. The Emperor asked: "What are their corresponding disasters?" Qibo said: "They each follow their transformations. Therefore, when the time comes, there are flourishing and waning; in encroachments, there is reversal or accordance; in remaining and guarding, there are more or fewer; in appearances, there are good or evil forms; among constellations, there are victories or defeats; thus, omens correspond to auspiciousness or misfortune." The Emperor asked: "What is meant by good and evil?" Qibo said: "There are joys and angers, sorrows and losses, moistures and dryness—these are the usual appearances. One must carefully observe them." The Emperor asked: "Are these six phenomena different in their heights or positions?" Qibo said: "Although the appearances may be high or low, their corresponding effects are one and the same; therefore, people also respond to them accordingly."

7
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	氣交變大... :	
帝曰：善。其德化政令之動靜損益，皆何如。歧伯曰：夫德化政令災變，不能相加也。勝復盛衰，不能相多也。往來小大，不能相過也。用之升降，不能相無也。各從其動而復之耳。帝曰：其病生何如。歧伯曰：德化者氣之祥，政令者氣之章，變易者復之紀，災眚者傷之始，氣相勝者和，不相勝者病，重感於邪，則甚也。帝曰：善。所謂精光之論，大聖之業，宣明大道，通於無窮，究於無極也。余聞之，善言天者，必應於人，善言古者，必驗於今，善言氣者，必彰於物，善言應者，同天地之化，善言化言變者，通神明之理。非夫子，孰能言至道歟。迺擇良兆而藏之靈室，每旦讀之，命曰氣交變，非齊戒不敢發，慎傳也。
		The Emperor said: "Well said." What are the effects on virtue, transformation, administration, and decrees—both in terms of activity or stillness, loss or gain? Qibo said: "Virtue, transformation, administration, decrees, and disasters cannot be simply added together." The dominance and restoration of excess or deficiency, flourishing or waning, cannot be measured in quantity. Their coming and going, minor or major, cannot surpass each other. Their application in rising or falling cannot be without effect. Each follows its movement and returns accordingly. The Emperor asked: "What are the diseases that arise from this?" Qibo said: "Virtue and transformation are auspicious signs of qi; administration and decrees are the manifestations of qi. Change and transformation mark the record of restoration; disasters and misfortunes signify the beginning of injury. When qis harmonize through mutual overcoming, there is balance; when they do not overcome each other, disease arises. If one is repeatedly affected by pathogenic influences, then the condition becomes severe." The Emperor said: "Well said." This is what is meant by the discourse on refined brilliance, the great achievement of a sage—proclaiming and clarifying the Great Dao, reaching into boundlessness, and penetrating to the infinite. I have heard that one who speaks well of Heaven must correspond to humanity; one who speaks well of antiquity must be verified in the present; one who speaks well about qi must manifest it through things; one who speaks skillfully about responses aligns with the transformations of Heaven and Earth. One who speaks insightfully about transformation and change understands the principles of the Divine and the Sage. If not for you, Master, who could speak of the ultimate Dao? Therefore, he selected auspicious omens and stored them in a sacred chamber. Each morning he read them, naming it "Qi Jiao Bian." Without solemn preparation and purification, one dared not open it—thus was the caution with which it was transmitted. 

70. 五常政大論 Great Discourse on the Five Constant Policies

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五常政大... :	
黃帝問曰：太虛寥廓，五運迴薄，衰盛不同，損益相從，願聞平氣何如而名，何如而紀也。歧伯對曰：昭乎哉問也。木曰敷和，火曰升明，土曰備化，金曰審平，水曰靜順。帝曰：其不及，奈何。歧伯曰：木曰委和，火曰伏明，土曰卑監，金曰從革，水曰涸流。帝曰：太過何謂。歧伯曰：木曰發生，火曰赫曦，上曰敦阜，金曰堅成，水曰流衍。帝曰：三氣之紀，願聞其候。歧伯曰：悉乎哉問也。敷和之紀，木德周行，陽舒陰布，五化宣平，其氣端，其性隨，其用曲直，其化生榮，其類草木，其政發散，其候溫和，其令風，其藏肝，肝其畏清，其主目，其穀麻，其果李，其實核，其應春，其蟲毛，其畜犬，其色蒼，其養筋，其病裏急支滿，其味酸，其音角，其物中堅，其數八。升明之紀，正陽而治，德施周普，五化均衡，其氣高，其性速，其用燔灼，其化蕃茂，其類火，其政明曜，其候炎暑，其令熱，其藏心，心其畏寒，其主舌，其穀麥，其果杏，其實絡，其應夏，其蟲羽，其畜馬，其色赤，其養血，其病瞤瘛，其味苦，其音徵，其物脈，其數七。備化之紀，氣協天休，德流四政，五化齊脩，其氣平，其性順，其用高下，其化豐滿，其類土，其政安靜，其候溽蒸，其令濕，其藏脾，脾其畏風，其主口，其穀稷，其果棗，其實肉，其應長夏，其蟲倮，其畜牛，其色黃，其養肉，其病否，其味甘，其音宮，其物膚，其數五。審平之紀，收而不爭，殺而無犯，五化宣明，其氣潔，其性剛，其用散落，其化堅斂，其類金，其政勁肅，其候清切，其令燥，其藏肺，肺其畏熱，其主鼻，其穀稻，其果桃，其實殼，其應秋，其蟲介，其畜難，其色白，其養皮毛，其病欬，其味辛，其音商，其物外堅，其數九。靜順之紀，藏而勿害，治而善下，五化咸整，其氣明，其性下，其用沃衍，其化凝堅，其類水，其政流演，其候凝肅，其令寒，其藏腎，腎其畏濕，其主二陰，其穀豆，其果栗，其實濡，其應冬，其蟲鱗，其畜彘，其色黑，其養骨髓，其病厥，其味鹹，其音羽，其物濡，其數六。故生而勿殺，長而勿罰，化而勿制，收而勿害，藏而勿抑，是謂平氣。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "The great void is vast and boundless, the Five Periodic Energies circulate and interact, their waxing and waning differ, losses and gains follow each other. I wish to hear how a balanced qi is named, and by what standard it is recorded." Qibo replied: "What an enlightened question this is!" Wood is called Fuhe, Fire is called Shengming, Earth is called Beihua, Metal is called Shenping, and Water is called Jingshun. The Emperor asked: "What if they are insufficient?" Qibo said: "Wood is called Weihe, Fire is called Fuming, Earth is called Bijian, Metal is called Congge, and Water is called Heliu." The Emperor asked: "What does it mean by excessive?" Qibo said: "Wood is called Fasheng, Fire is called Hexi, Earth is called Dunfu, Metal is called Jiancheng, and Water is called Liuyan." The Emperor said: "Regarding the three qi's records, I wish to hear about their signs." Qibo said: "What a thorough question this is!" The record of Fuhe is characterized by the virtue of Wood circulating universally. Yang expands and Yin spreads, the Five Transformations are harmoniously balanced. Its qi is upright; its nature follows easily; its function is to bend and stretch; its transformation brings forth growth and flourishing; its category includes grasses and trees; its governance is to disperse and release; its manifestation is mild warmth; its command is wind; its organ is the liver, which fears coldness; it governs the eyes; its grain is sesame; its fruit is plums; its actual form has a core; it corresponds to spring; its insect type is hairy; its domestic animal is dogs; its color is greenish-blue; it nourishes tendons; its disease manifests as internal tension and distention of the chest; its flavor is sour; its musical tone is Jiao (angle); its material quality is firm in the center; its number is eight. The record of Shengming is characterized by the governance of true Yang. Its virtue spreads universally and evenly, the Five Transformations maintain equilibrium. Its qi is lofty; its nature is rapid; its function is burning and scorching; its transformation brings abundant flourishing; its category belongs to Fire; its governance shines brightly; its manifestation is intense heat; its command is heat; its organ is the heart, which fears cold; it governs the tongue; its grain is wheat; its fruit is apricots; its actual form is net-like; it corresponds to summer; its insect type has wings; its domestic animal is horses; its color is red; it nourishes blood; its disease manifests as twitching and convulsions; its flavor is bitter; its musical tone is Zhi (sharp); its material quality relates to vessels; its number is seven. The record of Beihua is characterized by qi harmonizing with the celestial tranquility. Its virtue flows evenly in four directions, and the Five Transformations are equally cultivated. Its qi is balanced; its nature is compliant; its function varies between high and low; its transformation brings fullness and abundance; its category belongs to Earth; its governance is tranquil; its manifestation is humid and steaming heat; its command is dampness; its organ is the spleen, which fears wind; it governs the mouth; its grain is millet; its fruit is dates; its actual form is fleshy; it corresponds to late summer; its insect type is hairless; its domestic animal is cattle; its color is yellow; it nourishes flesh; its disease manifests as obstruction and stagnation; its flavor is sweet; its musical tone is Gong (palace); its material quality relates to the surface or skin; its number is five. The record of Shenping is characterized by gathering without contention, killing without transgression. The Five Transformations are clearly expressed and orderly. Its qi is pure; its nature is firm; its function is dispersing and falling; its transformation brings firmness and contraction; its category belongs to Metal; its governance is vigorous and solemn; its manifestation is clear and sharp cold; its command is dryness; its organ is the lungs, which fear heat; it governs the nose; its grain is rice; its fruit is peaches; its actual form has shells; it corresponds to autumn; its insect type is shelled or armored; its domestic animal is sheep; its color is white; it nourishes skin and hair; its disease manifests as coughing; its flavor is pungent; its musical tone is Shang (commercial); its material quality relates to outer firmness; its number is nine. The record of Jingshun is characterized by storing without harm, governing with humility and benevolence. The Five Transformations are all orderly arranged. Its qi is clear; its nature descends; its function brings nourishing abundance; its transformation results in condensation and firmness; its category belongs to Water; its governance flows smoothly; its manifestation is condensed solemnity; its command is cold; its organ is the kidneys, which fear dampness; it governs the two yins (genitalia); its grain is beans; its fruit is chestnuts; its actual form is moist; it corresponds to winter; its insect type has scales; its domestic animal is pigs; its color is black; it nourishes bones and marrow; its disease manifests as 厥 (jue, cold extremities or syncope); its flavor is salty; its musical tone is Yu (feather); its material quality relates to moisture; its number is six. Therefore, to promote life without killing, to foster growth without punishment, to transform without restriction, to gather without harm, and to store without suppression—this is called balanced qi.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
相關討論
	五常政大... :	
委和之紀，是謂勝生，生氣不政，化氣迺揚，長氣自平，收令迺早，涼雨時降，風雲並興，草木晚榮，蒼乾凋落，物秀而實，膚肉內充，其氣斂，其用聚，其動緛戾拘緩，其發驚駭，其藏肝，其果棗李，其實核殼，其穀稷稻，其味酸辛，其色白蒼，其畜犬雞，其蟲毛介，其主霧露淒滄，其聲角商，其病搖動注恐，從金化也，少角與判商同，上角與正角同，上商與正商同，其病支廢癰腫瘡瘍，其甘蟲，邪傷肝也，上宮與正宮同，蕭飋肅殺，則炎赫沸騰，眚於三，所謂復也，其主飛蠹蛆雉，迺為雷霆。伏明之紀，是謂勝長，長氣不宣，藏氣反布，收氣自政，化令迺衡，寒清數舉，暑令迺薄，承化物生，生而不長，成實而稚，遇化已老，陽氣屈伏，蟄蟲早藏，其氣鬱，其用暴，其動彰伏變易，其發痛，其藏心，其果栗桃，其實絡濡，其穀豆稻，其味苦鹹，其色玄丹，其畜馬彘，其蟲羽鱗，其主冰雪霜寒，其聲徵羽，其病昏惑悲忘，從水化也，少徵與少羽同，上商與正商同，邪傷心也，凝慘凓冽，則暴雨霖霪，眚於九，其主驟注雷霆震驚，沉霠淫雨。卑監之紀，是謂減化，化氣不令，生政獨彰，長氣整，雨迺愆，收氣平，風寒並興，草木榮美，秀而不實，成而粃也，其氣散，其用靜定，其動瘍涌分潰癰腫，其發濡滯，其藏脾，其果李栗，其實濡核，其穀豆麻，其味酸甘，其色蒼黃，其畜牛犬，其蟲倮毛，其主飄怒振發，其聲宮角，其病留滿否塞，從木化也，少宮與少角同，上宮與正宮同，上角與正角同，其病飱泄，邪傷脾也，振拉飄揚，則蒼乾散落，其眚四維，其主敗折虎狼，清氣迺用，生政西辱。從革之紀，是謂折收，收氣迺後，生氣迺揚，長化合德，火政迺宣，庶類以蕃，其氣揚，其用躁切，其動鏗禁瞀厥，其發欬喘，其藏肺，其果李杏，其實殼絡，其穀麻麥，其味苦辛，其色白丹，其畜雞羊，其蟲介羽，其主明曜炎爍，其聲商徵，其病嚏欬鼽衂，從火化也，少商與少徵同，上商與正商同，上角與正角同，邪傷肺也，炎光赫烈，則冰雪霜雹，眚於七，其主鱗伏彘鼠，歲氣早至，迺生大寒。涸流之紀，是謂反陽，藏令不舉，化氣迺昌，長氣宣布，蟄蟲不藏，土潤水泉減，草木條茂，榮秀滿盛，其氣滯，其用滲泄，其動堅止，其發燥槁，其藏腎，其果棗杏，其實濡肉，其穀黍稷，其味甘鹹，其色黅玄，其畜彘牛，其蟲鱗倮，其主埃鬱昏翳，其聲羽宮，其病痿厥堅下，從土化也，少羽與少宮同，上宮與正宮同，其病癃閟，邪傷腎也，埃昏驟雨，則振拉摧拔，眚於一，其主毛顯狐狢，變化不藏，故乘危而行，不速而至，暴虐無德，災反及之，微者復微，甚者復甚，氣之常也。
		The record of Weihe is called excessive Wood, which overcomes life. The vital qi does not govern properly; the transforming qi becomes prominent instead. The growing qi naturally balances itself, and the gathering command occurs early. Cool rains fall at appropriate times, while winds and clouds arise together. Grasses and trees flourish late, green dryness withers and falls, things appear beautiful yet bear fruit, flesh and muscles are internally 充实 (chongshi, filled). Its qi contracts; its function accumulates; its movement is twisted, constrained, and sluggish; its outbreak causes startlement and fear. Its organ is the liver; its fruits include dates and plums; their actual forms have cores or shells; its grains are millet and rice; its flavors are sour and pungent; its colors are white and greenish-blue; its domestic animals are dogs and chickens; its insects include hairy and shelled types; it governs mists, dews, coldness, and dampness; its sounds correspond to Jiao (angle) and Shang (commercial); the diseases manifest as shaking, movement disorders, and fear of falling—this follows the transformation of Metal. The minor Jiao combines with the minor Shang, while the upper Jiao aligns with the standard Jiao, and the upper Shang aligns with the standard Shang. Its diseases include limb atrophy, abscesses, swellings, and sores; its sweet worms are caused by pathogenic influences harming the liver. The upper Gong aligns with the standard Gong; when dryness becomes severe and killing is strict, fire will surge violently—this affects the third cycle, which is called a counteraction. Its governing creatures include flying insects, woodworms, maggots, and pheasants, and it manifests as thunder and lightning. The record of Fuming is called excessive Fire, which overcomes growth. The growing qi does not spread properly; the storing qi instead spreads outward. The gathering qi governs itself, and the transforming command achieves balance. Cold and coolness frequently arise, while heat becomes weak. Things receive transformation but do not grow fully; they bear fruit yet remain immature. When encountering transformation, they are already aged. Yang qi is suppressed and hidden, insects hibernate early. Its qi accumulates; its function acts violently; its movement alternates between manifesting and concealing, changing unpredictably; its outbreak causes pain. Its organ is the heart; its fruits include chestnuts and peaches; their actual forms are net-like or moist; its grains are beans and rice; its flavors are bitter and salty; its colors are dark black and red; its domestic animals are horses and pigs; its insects have wings and scales; it governs snow, ice, frost, and coldness; its sounds correspond to Zhi (sharp) and Yu (feather); the diseases manifest as confusion, delirium, sadness, and forgetfulness—this follows the transformation of Water. The minor Zhi aligns with the minor Yu, while the upper Shang corresponds to the standard Shang; pathogenic influences harm the heart. When coldness becomes severe and chilling winds arise, torrential rains and continuous downpours will occur—this affects the ninth cycle, which is called a counterreaction. Its governing phenomena include sudden heavy rain, thunder, lightning, and shocks, as well as dense drizzle and prolonged rainfall. The record of Bijian is called diminished transformation, where the transforming qi does not command properly; instead, the vital qi alone becomes prominent. The growing qi remains orderly, but rains become irregular. The gathering qi is balanced, and wind and cold arise together. Grasses and trees appear beautiful and flourishing, yet they bear no fruit or produce inferior results. Its qi disperses; its function remains static and stable; its movement causes sores to swell, burst open, and form abscesses; its outbreak manifests as dampness and stagnation. Its organ is the spleen; its fruits include plums and chestnuts; their actual forms are moist or have cores; its grains are beans and sesame; its flavors are sour and sweet; its colors are greenish-blue and yellow; its domestic animals are cattle and dogs; its insects are hairless and hairy types; it governs drifting winds, violent outbursts, and shaking; its sounds correspond to Gong (palace) and Jiao (angle); the diseases manifest as retention, fullness, obstruction, and blockage—this follows the transformation of Wood. The minor Gong aligns with the minor Jiao, while the upper Gong corresponds to the standard Gong, and the upper Jiao corresponds to the standard Jiao. Its disease manifests as diarrhea from overeating; pathogenic influences harm the spleen. When shaking and pulling forces arise along with drifting winds, green dryness will scatter and fall—this affects all four directions (the Four Directions), its governing phenomena include broken branches, tigers, wolves, and other destructive forces, while clear qi becomes active, causing the vital function to be dishonored in the west. The record of Congge is called excessive Metal, which overcomes gathering. The gathering qi lags behind, while the vital qi becomes prominent; the growing and transforming qi combine harmoniously, and fire governance spreads widely. All things flourish abundantly. Its qi rises upward; its function is restless and urgent; its movement causes metallic sounds, confusion, and fainting or syncope; its outbreak manifests as coughing and asthma. Its organ is the lungs; its fruits include plums and apricots; their actual forms have shells or net-like structures; its grains are sesame and wheat; its flavors are bitter and pungent; its colors combine white and red; its domestic animals include chickens and sheep; its insects include shelled and winged types; it governs bright radiance, blazing firelight; its sounds correspond to Shang (commercial) and Zhi (sharp); the diseases manifest as sneezing, coughing, nasal discharge, and nosebleeds—this follows the transformation of Fire. The minor Shang aligns with the minor Zhi, while the upper Shang matches the standard Shang, and the upper Jiao matches the standard Jiao; pathogenic influences harm the lungs. When firelight becomes intense and blazing, snow, ice, frost, hail will occur—this affects the seventh cycle, which is called a counteracting phenomenon. Its governing phenomena include scaled creatures hiding, pigs, rats, and early arrival of annual qi, resulting in severe cold. The record of Heliu is called excessive Water, which reverses Yang. The storing command does not arise; the transforming qi flourishes instead. The growing qi spreads outward, and hibernating insects do not hide. Earth becomes moist while water sources diminish; grasses and trees grow luxuriantly with abundant flowering and fruiting. Its qi stagnates; its function involves seeping and discharging; its movement is firm yet stagnant; its outbreak manifests as dryness and desiccation. Its organ is the kidneys; its fruits include dates and apricots; their forms are moist and fleshy; its grains are millet, yellow millet (shu), and j 稷 (ji); its flavors are sweet and salty; its colors combine yellowish-black and dark black; its domestic animals are pigs and cattle; its insects include scaled and hairless types; it governs dust, stagnation, dimness, and obscurity; its sounds correspond to Yu (feather) and Gong (palace); the diseases manifest as atrophy, syncope or fainting, and firmness in the lower body—this follows the transformation of Earth. The minor Yu aligns with the minor Gong, while the upper Gong matches the standard Gong. Its disease manifests as urinary retention and blockage; pathogenic influences harm the kidneys. When dust obscures the sky and sudden rains arise, shaking forces will uproot and destroy things—this affects the first cycle, which is called a countereffect. Its governing phenomena include hairy creatures such as foxes and badgers, whose transformations are not concealed, so they act recklessly without caution or speed. Their violent and cruel nature lacks virtue, leading to disasters that return upon them. The mild will become even milder, while the severe will become more extreme—this is the normal course of qi.

	五常政大... :	
發生之紀，是謂啟𢽬，土踈泄，蒼氣達，陽和布化，陰氣迺隨，生氣淳化，萬物以榮，其化生，其氣美，其政散，其令條舒，其動掉眩巔疾，其德鳴靡啟坼，其變振拉摧拔，其穀麻稻，其畜雞犬，其果李桃，其色青黃白，其味酸甘辛，其象春，其經足厥陰少陽，其藏肝脾，其蟲毛介，其物中堅外堅，其病怒，太角與上商同，上徵則其氣逆，其病吐利，不務其德，則收氣復，秋氣勁切，甚則肅殺，清氣大至，草木凋零，邪迺傷肝。赫曦之紀，是謂蕃茂，陰氣內化，陽氣外榮，炎暑施化，物得以昌，其化長，其氣高，其政動，其令鳴顯，其動炎灼妄擾，其德暄暑鬱蒸，其變炎烈沸騰，其穀麥豆，其畜羊彘，其果杏栗，其色赤白玄，其味苦辛鹹，其象夏，其經手少陰太陽，手厥陰少陽，其藏心肺，其蟲羽鱗，其物脈濡，其病笑瘧瘡瘍血流狂妄目赤，上羽與正徵同，其收齊，其病痓，上徵而收氣後也，暴烈其政，藏氣迺復，時見凝慘，甚則雨水霜雹切寒，邪傷心也。敦阜之紀，是謂廣化，厚德清靜，順長以盈，至陰內實，物化充成，煙埃朦鬱，見於厚土，大雨時行，濕氣迺用，燥政迺辟，其化圓，其氣豐，其政靜，其令周備，其動濡積并蓄，其德柔潤重淖，其變震驚飄驟崩潰，其穀稷麻，其畜牛犬，其果棗李，其色黅玄蒼，其味甘鹹酸，其象長夏，其經足太陰陽明，其藏脾腎，其蟲倮毛，其物肌核，其病腹滿，四支不舉，大風迅至，邪傷脾也，堅成之紀，是謂收引，天氣潔，地氣明，陽氣隨，陰治化，燥行其政，物以司成，收氣繁布，化洽不終，其化成，其氣削，其政肅，其令銳切，其動暴折瘍疰，其德霧露蕭飋，其變肅殺凋零，其穀稻黍，其畜雞馬，其果桃杏，其色白青丹，其味辛酸苦，其象秋，其經手太陰陽明，其藏肺肝，其蟲介羽，其物殼絡，其病喘喝胸憑仰息，上徵與正商同，其生齊，其病欬，政暴變，則名木不榮，柔脆焦首，長氣斯救，大火流，炎爍且至，蔓將槁，邪傷肺也。流衍之紀，是謂封藏，寒司物化，天地嚴凝，藏政以布，長令不揚，其化凜，其氣堅，其政謐，其令流注，其動漂泄沃涌，其德凝慘寒雰，其變冰雪霜雹，其穀豆稷，其畜彘牛，其果栗棗，其色黑丹黅，其味鹹苦甘，其象冬，其經足少陰太陽，其藏腎心，其蟲鱗倮，其物濡滿，其病脹，上羽而長氣不化也，政過則化氣大舉而埃昏氣交，大雨時降，邪傷腎也。故曰：不恒其德，則所勝來復，政恒其理，則所勝同化，此之謂也。
		The record of Fasheng is called the beginning of birth, where Earth becomes loose and moist; green qi spreads widely. Yang warmth distributes transformation, while Yin qi follows accordingly. The vital qi becomes pure and transformative, bringing flourishing to all things. Its transformation brings life; its qi is harmonious; its governance disperses outward; its command causes orderly growth; its movement results in dizziness, vertigo, and sudden illness; its virtue manifests as rustling sounds, softness, and the opening of cracks; its changes involve shaking, pulling, uprooting, and destruction. Its grains are sesame and rice; its domestic animals include chickens and dogs; its fruits are plums and peaches; its colors combine green, yellow, and white; its flavors are sour, sweet, and pungent; it corresponds to spring; it relates to the foot Jueyin and Shaoyang meridians; its organs are the liver and spleen; its insects include hairy and scaled types; its material quality combines inner firmness with outer firmness; the disease manifests as anger. The major Jiao aligns with the upper Shang, while an excessive Zhi causes qi to reverse upward, resulting in vomiting and diarrhea. If one neglects virtue, then gathering qi will return, autumn's sharp and cutting energy becomes strong, and if severe, it leads to 肃杀 (susha, killing and destruction), as clear coldness arrives in great force, causing grasses and trees to wither and fall—pathogenic influences then harm the liver. The record of Hexi is called flourishing abundance, where Yin qi transforms internally and Yang qi flourishes externally. Blazing heat spreads transformation, allowing things to thrive. Its transformation promotes growth; its qi rises high; its governance acts dynamically; its command manifests as loud and clear sounds. Its movement causes burning, disturbance, and confusion; its virtue brings warmth, heat, and humid steaming; its changes manifest as intense fire, boiling surges. Its grains are wheat and beans; its domestic animals include sheep and pigs; its fruits are apricots and chestnuts; its colors combine red, white, and black; its flavors are bitter, pungent, and salty; it corresponds to summer; it relates to the hand Shaoyin Taiyang and foot Jueyin Shaoyang meridians. Its organs are the heart and lungs; its insects include winged and scaled types; its material qualities relate to vessels and moisture; the diseases manifest as laughter, malaria, sores, bleeding, madness, delirium, red eyes. The upper Yu aligns with the standard Zhi; gathering reaches balance, and disease manifests as spasticity (shizheng). When excessive Zhi arises, gathering qi follows later. If governance becomes violent and intense, storing qi will return; at times coldness and condensation appear, and if severe, rain, water, frost, hail, and sharp cold occur—pathogenic influences harm the heart. The record of Dunfu is called extensive transformation, where virtue becomes thick and tranquil. It follows the growth process to reach fullness; Yin reaches internal solidity, allowing all things to transform into completion. Smoke and dust become dense and obscure, appearing in thick earth. Heavy rains fall at appropriate times; damp qi becomes active while dry governance recedes. Its transformation is circular; its qi abundant; its governance tranquil; its command comprehensive and complete. Its movement involves moist accumulation and concurrent storage; its virtue brings softness, moisture, and heavy wetness; its changes manifest as earthquakes, sudden winds, collapses, and destructions. Its grains are millet and sesame; its domestic animals include cattle and dogs; its fruits include dates and plums. Its colors combine yellow-black, black, and greenish-blue; its flavors are sweet, salty, and sour; it corresponds to late summer. It relates to the foot Taiyin Yangming meridians; its organs include the spleen and kidneys; its insects include hairless and hairy types. Its material qualities involve flesh and cores; diseases manifest as abdominal distension, inability of limbs to move, sudden strong winds arriving—pathogenic influences harm the spleen. The record of Jiancheng is called gathering and contraction, where Heaven's qi becomes clean, Earth's qi clear, Yang follows along, Yin governs transformation. Dryness executes its governance, allowing things to reach completion. Gathering qi spreads widely; transformation harmonizes but does not conclude. Its transformation results in completion; its qi diminishes; its governance is solemn; its command sharp and precise. Its movement causes violent breaking, sores, and abscesses. Its virtue brings mists, dews, and dry winds; its changes manifest as killing, destruction, withering, and falling. Its grains are rice and yellow millet (shu); its domestic animals include chickens and horses; its fruits include peaches and apricots. Its colors combine white, green, and red; its flavors are pungent, sour, and bitter; it corresponds to autumn. It relates to the hand Taiyin Yangming meridians. Its organs include the lungs and liver; its insects include shelled or winged types. Its material qualities relate to shells and net-like forms. Diseases manifest as coughing with chest oppression and labored breathing. The upper Zhi aligns with standard Shang, where vital qi reaches balance; diseases manifest as cough. If governance changes violently, then tall trees will not flourish, soft and brittle plants will dry at the top, and growing qi must intervene to rescue. Great fire flows, blazing heat approaches, and grasses will soon wither—pathogenic influences harm the lungs. The record of Liuyan is called sealing and storing, where cold governs the transformation of all things; Heaven and Earth become severe and condensed. Storing governance spreads outward while growing commands do not flourish. Its transformation becomes cold and sharp; its qi firm; its governance quiet; its command involves flowing and pouring. Its movement causes drifting discharges, moist surges, and overflowing waters. Its virtue manifests as condensation, desolation, and chilling mists; its changes include snow, ice, frost, and hail. Its grains are beans and millet; its domestic animals include pigs and cattle; its fruits include chestnuts and dates. Its colors combine black, red, and yellow-black; its flavors are salty, bitter, and sweet; it corresponds to winter. It relates to the foot Shaoyin Taiyang meridians; its organs involve the kidneys and heart; its insects include scaled or hairless types. Its material quality is moist and full; diseases manifest as swelling. When upper Yu arises but growing qi does not transform properly, if governance becomes excessive, transforming qi will surge strongly, causing dust and dimness to intermingle with the air, heavy rains falling at appropriate times—pathogenic influences harm the kidneys. Therefore it is said: "If virtue does not remain constant, the overcomer will return to counteract; if governance remains consistent with its principles, then the overcomer and the overcomee will transform together." This is what is meant.

	五常政大... :	
帝曰：天不足西北，左寒而右涼，地不滿東南，右熱而左溫，其故何也。歧伯曰：陰陽之氣，高下之理，太少之異也。東南方，陽也，陽者其精降於下，故右熱而左溫。西北方，陰也，陰者其精奉於上，故左寒而右涼。是以地有高下，氣有溫涼，高者氣寒，下者氣熱。故適寒涼者脹之，溫熱者瘡，下之則脹已，汗之則瘡已，此湊理開閉之常，太少之異耳。帝曰：其於壽夭何如。歧伯曰：陰精所奉其人壽，陽精所降其人夭。帝曰：善。其病也，治之柰何。歧伯曰：西北之氣散而寒之，東南之氣收而溫之，所謂同病異治也。故曰：氣寒氣涼，治以寒涼，行水漬之。氣溫氣熱，治以溫熱，強其內守。必同其氣，可使平也，假者反之。
		The Emperor asked: "Heaven lacks fullness in the northwest, with cold on the left and coolness on the right; Earth is insufficient in the southeast, with heat on the right and warmth on the left. What is the reason for this?" Qibo said: "This is due to the qi of Yin and Yang, the principle of elevation and depression, and the differences between excess and deficiency." The southeast is Yang; where Yang exists, its essence descends downward, hence heat on the right side and warmth on the left side. The northwest is Yin; where Yin exists, its essence rises upward, hence coldness on the left and coolness on theright. Therefore, Earth has elevations and depressions, and qi varies in warmth and coolness; higher places have cold qi, while lower areas have warm qi. Therefore, those who are exposed to cold and coolness will experience distension; those affected by warmth and heat may develop sores. Purging relieves distension, while sweating alleviates sores—this is the usual pattern of opening and closing in the pores, merely a difference between excess and deficiency. The Emperor asked: "How does this relate to longevity or early death?" Qibo said: "Those who receive Yin essence live long, while those affected by the descent of Yang essence die young." The Emperor said: "Good." What about the treatment of these diseases? Qibo said: "For conditions in the northwest, disperse and warm against cold; for those in the southeast, gather and apply warmth. This is what is called treating the same disease with different methods." Therefore it is said: "For cold or cool qi, treat with cold and cooling therapies; use water to moisten and permeate." "For warm or hot qi, treat with warmth and heat, strengthening the body's internal defenses." It is essential to harmonize with its nature of qi; only then can balance be achieved. In cases of false or counteracting conditions, the opposite approach should be taken.

	五常政大... :	
帝曰：善。一州之氣生化壽夭不同，其故何也。歧伯曰：高下之理地勢使然也。崇高則陰氣治之，污下則陽氣治之，陽勝者先天，陰勝者後天，此地理之常，生化之道也。帝曰：其有壽夭乎。歧伯曰：高者其氣壽，下者其氣夭，地之小大異也，小者小異，大者大異。故治病者，必明天道地理，陰陽更勝，氣之先後，人之壽夭，生化之期，乃可以知人之形氣矣。
		The Emperor said: "Good." Why do the vital transformations, longevity, and early death differ among people in one region? What is the reason? Qibo said: "This is due to the principle of elevation and depression determined by the terrain." High and elevated areas are governed by Yin qi, while low-lying and damp regions are governed by Yang qi. Those with excessive Yang are born with innate advantages; those with excessive Yin develop later in life. This is the normal pattern of geography and the principle of vital transformation. The Emperor asked: "Do longevity and early death exist as a result?" Qibo said: "Those in high places have long life, while those in low-lying areas tend to die young. This difference depends on the size of the terrain; minor differences lead to small variations, and major differences result in great disparities." Therefore, those who treat diseases must understand the principles of Heaven's Dao and Earth's geography, the alternating dominance of Yin and Yang, the sequence of qi, human longevity or early death, and the timing of vital transformations. Only then can they truly comprehend a person's physical form and qi.

	五常政大... :	
帝曰：善。其歲有不病，而藏氣不應不用者，何也。歧伯曰：天氣制之，氣有所從也。帝曰：願卒聞之。歧伯曰：少陽司天，火氣下臨，肺氣上從，白起金用，草木眚，火見燔焫，革金且耗，大暑以行，欬嚏鼽衂鼻窒，曰瘍，寒熱胕腫。風行于地，塵沙飛揚，心痛胃脘痛，厥逆鬲不通，其主暴速。陽明司天，燥氣下臨，肝氣上從，蒼起木用而立，土迺眚，淒滄數至，木伐草萎，脇痛目赤，掉振鼓慄，筋痿不能久立。暴熱至，土迺暑，陽氣鬱發痛，小便變，寒熱如瘧，甚則心痛，火行于稿，流水不冰，蟄蟲迺見。太陽司天，寒氣下臨，心氣上從，而火且明，丹起金迺眚，寒清時舉，勝則水冰，火氣高明，心熱煩嗌乾，善渴鼽嚏，喜悲數欠，熱氣妄行，寒迺復，霜不時降，善忘甚則心痛。土迺潤，水豐衍，寒客至，沈陰化濕，氣變物水飲內蓄，中滿不食，皮𤸷肉苛，筋脈不利，甚則胕腫身後癰。厥陰司天，風氣下臨，脾氣上從，而土且隆，黃起水迺眚，土用革，體重肌肉萎，食減口爽，風行太虛，雲物搖動，目轉耳鳴。火縱其暴，地迺暑，大熱消爍，赤沃下，蟄蟲數見，流水不冰，其發機速。少陰司天，熱氣下臨，肺氣上從，白起金用，草木眚，喘嘔寒熱嚏鼽衂鼻窒，大暑流行，甚則瘡瘍燔灼，金爍石流。地迺燥清，淒滄數至，脇痛善太息，肅殺行，草木變。太陰司天，濕氣下臨，腎氣上從，黑起水變，埃冒雲雨，胸中不利，陰痿氣大衰而不起不用。當其時反腰脽痛，動轉不便也。厥逆，地迺藏陰，大寒且至，蟄蟲早附，心下否痛，地裂冰堅，少腹痛時害於食，乘金則止水增，味迺鹹，行水減也。
		The Emperor said: "Good." Why are there years when people do not fall ill, and the organs' qi does not respond or act? What is the reason for that? Qibo said: "This is due to Heaven's qi controlling it, and the qi follows a certain path." The Emperor said: "I wish to hear this in full." Qibo said: "When Shaoyang governs Heaven, Fire qi descends to the lower regions, while Lung qi rises in response. White phenomena arise and Metal functions become active; grasses and trees suffer damage. Fire appears as burning and scorching, transforming Metal into exhaustion. Great heat prevails, causing coughing, sneezing, nasal discharge, nosebleeds, and nasal obstruction—this is called sores (yangchuang), along with alternating cold and heat and swelling." Winds move across the Earth, raising dust and sand into the air. This causes heart pain, epigastric distress, reversal of qi (厥逆), obstruction at the diaphragm, and its manifestations are sudden and rapid in onset. When Yangming governs Heaven, dryness descends to the lower region, while Liver qi rises in response. Green phenomena arise and Wood functions become active; Earth suffers damage. Cold and damp conditions frequently occur, trees are cut down, grasses wither, flank pain and red eyes appear, shaking and trembling manifest, tendons atrophy, and one is unable to stand for long periods. Sudden heat arrives, Earth becomes hot and humid; Yang qi accumulates and causes pain. Urine changes in nature; alternating cold and fever occur like malaria, and severe cases result in heart pain. Fire spreads among withered things; flowing water does not freeze, and hibernating creatures emerge. When Taiyang governs Heaven, cold qi descends to the lower region while Heart qi rises in response; Fire becomes prominent. Red phenomena arise and Metal suffers damage. Coldness and clarity frequently appear; if excessive, water freezes. Fire qi is high and bright, causing heart heat, irritability, dry throat, frequent thirst, nasal discharge, sneezing, sadness, frequent sighing, erratic movement of Heat qi, followed by a return of cold. Frost falls at irregular times, leading to forgetfulness; in severe cases, it results in heart pain. Earth becomes moist, water is abundant and widespread. Cold pathogenic influences arrive; deep Yin transforms into dampness. Qi changes, causing internal retention of fluids—this results in abdominal distension and lack of appetite, skin itching, flesh discomfort, impaired tendons and vessels, and in severe cases, swelling and abscesses on the back of the body. When Jueyin governs Heaven, wind qi descends to the lower realm while Spleen qi rises in response; Earth becomes prominent. Yellow phenomena arise and Water suffers damage. The function of Earth transforms, causing body heaviness, muscle atrophy, reduced appetite, and a clear mouth sensation. Wind moves through the great void, clouds and objects sway, eyes rotate, and ears ring. Fire spreads violently; Earth becomes hot. Intense heat scorches and dries, red fertile land descends, hibernating creatures frequently appear, flowing water does not freeze—its onset is rapid. When Shaoyin governs Heaven, heat qi descends to the lower realms while Lung qi rises in response; white phenomena arise and Metal functions are active. Grasses and trees suffer damage; coughing with vomiting, alternating cold and fever, sneezing, nasal discharges, nosebleeds, nasal obstruction occur. Great heat spreads widely, and in severe cases sores appear as if burned—Metal scorches and stones flow. The Earth becomes dry and clear; cold and damp conditions frequently arise, causing flank pain and frequent sighing.肃杀 (killing and destruction) prevails, transforming grasses and trees. When Taiyin governs Heaven, dampness descends to the lower regions while Kidney qi rises in response; black phenomena arise and Water undergoes transformation. Dust obscures clouds and rain, causing discomfort in the chest, Yin atrophy, great decline of qi with inability to rise or function properly. At that time, there is sudden pain in the waist and hips, and difficulty moving or turning. Reversal of qi (厥逆) occurs, and Earth stores Yin internally; great cold is about to arrive. Hibernating creatures seek shelter early. There is obstruction and pain beneath the heart. The ground cracks and ice becomes firm. Lower abdominal pain affects eating at times. When Metal prevails, water increases; flavors become salty, while diuretic effects decrease.

顯示相似段落
	五常政大... :	
帝曰：歲有胎孕不育，治之不全，何氣使然。歧伯曰：六氣五類，有相勝制也，同者盛之，異者衰之，此天地之道，生化之常也。故厥陰司天，毛蟲靜，羽蟲育，介蟲不成，在泉，毛蟲育，倮蟲耗，羽蟲不育。少陰司天，羽蟲靜，介蟲育，毛蟲不成，在泉，羽蟲育，介蟲耗不育。太陰司天，倮蟲靜，鱗蟲育，羽蟲不成，在泉，倮蟲育，鱗蟲不成。少陽司天，羽蟲靜，毛蟲育，倮蟲不成，在泉，羽蟲育，介蟲耗，毛蟲不育。陽明司天，介蟲靜，羽蟲育，介蟲不成，在泉，介蟲育，毛蟲耗，羽蟲不成。太陽司天，鱗蟲靜，倮蟲育，在泉，鱗蟲耗，倮蟲不育。諸乘所不成之運，則甚也。故氣主有所制，歲立有所生，地氣制己勝，天氣制勝己，天制色，地制形，五類衰盛，各隨其氣之所宜也。故有胎孕不育治之不全，此氣之常也，所謂中根也。根于外者亦五，故生化之別，有五氣五味五色五類五宜也。帝曰：何謂也。歧伯曰：根于中者，命曰神機，神去則機息根于外者，命曰氣立，氣止則化絕。故各有制，各有勝，各有生，各有成。故曰：不知年之所加，氣之同異，不足以言生化，此之謂也。
		The Emperor asked: "In some years, pregnancies do not result in birth or treatment is incomplete—what qi causes this?" Qibo said: "The Six Qi and Five Categories have mutual overcoming relationships. When they are the same, they flourish; when different, they decline. This is the principle of Heaven and Earth, the normal process of life and transformation." Therefore, when Jueyin governs the heavens, hairy creatures remain still; winged creatures reproduce; shelled creatures fail to mature. In the lower regions (zaiquan), hairy creatures reproduce; hairless creatures are exhausted; winged creatures do not reproduce. When Shaoyin governs the heavens, winged creatures remain still; shelled creatures reproduce; hairy creatures fail to mature. In lower regions (zaiquan), winged creatures reproduce while shelled creatures are exhausted and do not reproduce. When Taiyin governs the heavens, hairless creatures remain still; scaled creatures reproduce; winged creatures fail to mature. In lower realms (zaiquan), hairless creatures reproduce while scaled creatures do not mature. When Shaoyang governs the heavens, winged insects remain still; hairy creatures reproduce; hairless ones fail to mature. In lower domains (zaiquan), winged insects reproduce while shelled ones are exhausted and hairy ones do not reproduce. When Yangming governs the heavens, shelled creatures remain still; winged ones reproduce; but shelled ones fail to mature. In zaiquan (lower regions), shelled creatures reproduce while hairy ones are exhausted and winged ones do not mature. When Taiyang governs the heavens, scaled creatures remain still while hairless ones reproduce. In zaiquan (the lower regions), scaled creatures are exhausted and hairless ones do not reproduce. Those who encounter the years in which their corresponding category of creature cannot mature will suffer severe conditions. Therefore, the governing qi has its constraints; each year establishes what can flourish. Earth's qi controls that which it overcomes; Heaven's qi governs that which overcomes it. Heaven determines color, while Earth determines form. The waxing and waning of the Five Categories follow their respective suitability to the prevailing qi. Therefore, there are cases where pregnancy does not result in birth or healing is incomplete—this is a normal occurrence of qi. This is referred to as "middle root" (zhonggen). Rooted in the external environment are also five factors; therefore, distinctions in life and transformation include Five Qi, Five Flavors, Five Colors, Five Categories, and Five Appropriatenesses. The Emperor asked: "What do you mean by this?" Qibo said: "That which is rooted internally is called Shenji (divine mechanism); when the spirit departs, the mechanism ceases. That which is rooted externally is called Qili (qi establishment); when qi stops, transformation ends." Therefore, each has its own regulation, its own overcoming relationship, its own generation, and its own completion. Hence it is said: "Without knowing the years' influences, the similarities and differences of qi, one cannot speak about life and transformation." This is what is meant.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五常政大... :	
帝曰：氣始而生化，氣散而有形，氣布而蕃育，氣終而象變，其致一也。然而五味所資，生化有薄厚，成熟有少多，終始不同，其故何也。歧伯曰：地氣制之也，非天不生，地不長也。帝曰：願聞其道。歧伯曰：寒熱燥濕，不同其化也。故少陽在泉，寒毒不生，其味辛，其治苦酸，其穀蒼丹。陽明在泉，濕毒不生，其味酸，其氣濕，其治辛苦甘，其穀丹素。太陽在泉，熱毒不生，其味苦，其治淡鹹，其穀黅秬。厥陰在泉，清毒不生，其味甘，其治酸苦，其穀蒼赤，其氣專，其味正。少陰在泉，寒毒不生，其味辛，其治辛苦甘，其穀白丹。太陰在泉，燥毒不生，其味鹹，其其氣熱，其治甘鹹，其榖黅秬。化淳則鹹守，氣專則辛化而俱治。
		The Emperor said: "Qi begins and gives rise to transformation; qi disperses and forms physical shapes; qi spreads and promotes proliferation; qi concludes and produces changes in form. All of these lead to the same fundamental principle." Yet, depending on the nourishment from the Five Flavors, the degree of transformation and generation varies in depth or shallowness; maturation differs in quantity—some produce little, others much. The beginning and end differ. What is the reason for this? Qibo said: "This is determined by Earth's qi. Without Heaven, there would be no generation; without Earth, nothing would grow." The Emperor said: "I wish to hear the principle behind this." Qibo said: "Cold, heat, dryness, and damp differ in their transformations." Therefore, when Shaoyang governs zaiquan (the lower region), cold toxins do not arise. Its flavor is pungent; the appropriate treatment uses bitter and sour flavors; its grains are greenish-blue or red in color. When Yangming governs zaiquan, damp toxins do not arise; its flavor is sour. Its qi is damp; the appropriate treatment uses pungent, bitter, and sweet flavors; its grains are red or white in color. When Taiyang governs zaiquan, heat toxins do not arise; its flavor is bitter. The appropriate treatment uses bland and salty flavors; its grains are yellow-black or black in color. When Jueyin governs zaiquan, clear toxins do not arise; its flavor sweet. The appropriate treatment uses sour and bitter flavors; its grains are green or red in color; its qi is singular and focused, and its flavor is pure. When Shaoyin governs zaiquan (lower region), cold toxins do not occur; its flavor is pungent. The appropriate treatment involves pungent, bitter and sweet flavors; the grains are white or red in color. When Taiyin governs zaiquan (Lower Region), dry toxins do not arise; its taste is salty. Its qi is hot; the appropriate treatment involves sweet and salty flavors, and its grains are yellow-black or jet black in color. When transformation becomes pure, salt maintains stability; when qi is focused, pungency transforms and all treatments harmonize accordingly.

9
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	五常政大... :	
故曰：補上下者從之，治上下者逆之，以所在寒熱盛衰而調之。故曰：上取下取，內取外取，以求其過。能毒者以厚藥，不勝毒者以薄藥，此之謂也。氣反者，病在上，取之下，病在下，取之上，病在中，傍取之。治熱以寒，溫而行之，治寒以熱，涼而行之，治溫以清，冷而行之，治清以溫，熱而行之。故消之削之，吐之下之，補之寫之，久新同法。帝曰：病在中，而不實不堅，且聚且散，柰何。歧伯曰：悉乎哉問也。無積者求其藏，虛則補之，藥以袪之，食以隨之，行水漬之，和其中外，可使畢已。帝曰：有毒無毒，服有約乎。歧伯曰：病有久新，方有大小，有毒無毒，固宜常制矣。大毒治病，十去其六，常毒治病，十去其七，小毒治病，十去其八，無毒治病，十去其九。穀肉果菜，食養盡之，無使過之，傷其正也。不盡，行復如法，必先歲氣，無伐天和，無盛盛，無虛虛，而遺人天殃，無致邪，無失正，絕人長命。帝曰：其久病者，有氣從不康，病去而瘠，柰何。歧伯曰：昭乎哉聖人之問也。化不可代，時不可違。夫經絡以通，血氣以從，復其不足，與眾齊同，養之和之，靜以待時，謹守其氣，無使傾移，其形迺彰，生氣以長，命曰聖王。故大要曰：無代化，無違時，必養必和，待其來復，此之謂也。帝曰：善。
		Therefore it is said: "To nourish the upper and lower regions, follow their nature; to treat disorders in these regions, go against them. Adjust according to the prevailing coldness or heat, waxing or waning." Therefore it is said: "Treatment may involve taking from above or below, within or without, in order to identify and correct the excesses." Those who can tolerate strong effects should be given potent medicines; those who cannot withstand toxicity should receive mild ones. This is what it means. When the qi acts in opposition, if the disease is above, treat below; if the disease is below, treat above; if it is in the middle, treat from the sides. To treat heat, use cold; apply it gently and gradually. To treat cold, use warmth; apply it with cooling moderation. To treat warmth, use clearing (cooling); apply it with coolness. To treat clear (cold) conditions, use warming; apply it with heat. Therefore, whether the condition is recent or long-standing, the methods of elimination, reduction, emesis, purgation, supplementation, and drainage follow the same principles. The Emperor asked: "When a disease is in the middle region but not solid or firm, yet gathers and disperses intermittently, how should it be treated?" Qibo said: "What an insightful question this is!" For those without accumulation, seek the affected organ; if deficiency exists, supplement it. Use medicine to expel pathogenic influences and follow with appropriate diet. Employ water-based therapies for moistening, harmonizing internal and external conditions—this can lead to complete recovery. The Emperor asked: "Are there medicines that are toxic or non-toxic? Is there a limit or regulation for their administration?" Qibo said: "Diseases vary in duration, old or new; prescriptions differ in strength. Whether a medicine is toxic or non-toxic, there should certainly be standard regulations." Medicines with strong toxicity can remove 60% of the disease; those with moderate toxicity, 70%; those with mild toxicity, 80%; and non-toxic medicines, 90%. Grains, meats, fruits, and vegetables should be used to nourish fully; do not overuse them, for this would harm the healthy qi. If the condition is not fully resolved, continue treatment according to established methods. Always first consider the annual qi; do not disrupt natural harmony. Do not excessively treat excess conditions or further weaken deficiency, for this would bring heavenly calamity upon people. Avoid inviting pathogenic influences and do not lose the body's normal balance—this preserves long life. The Emperor asked: "For those with chronic illness, whose qi does not recover and who remain emaciated even after the disease has departed, how should they be treated?" Qibo said: "How clear and profound is the question of a sage!" Transformation cannot be forced; timing must not be defied. When the meridians and collaterals are unblocked, blood and qi can flow freely. Supplement what is deficient so that the person returns to a state of balance with others. Nourish and harmonize, remaining calm while awaiting the right time. Carefully preserve their vital energy, preventing it from being disturbed or displaced; then the body will manifest health, life force will flourish, and this is called the way of a sage-king. Therefore it is said in the essential principles: "Do not force transformation, do not defy timing. Necessarily nourish and harmonize; await their natural recovery." This is what is meant by that statement. The Emperor said: "Well said." 

71. 六元正紀大論 Liu Yuan Zheng Ji Da Lun: The Great Discourse on the Six Fundamental Principles and Their Regular Patterns

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
黃帝問曰：六化六變，勝復淫治，甘苦辛鹹酸淡先後，余知之矣。夫五運之化，或從五氣，或逆五氣，或從天氣而逆地氣，或從地氣而逆天氣，或相得，或不相得，余未能明其事。欲通天之紀，從地之理，和其運，調其化，使上下合德，無相奪倫，天地升降，不失其宜，五運宣行，勿乖其政，調之正味，從逆柰何。歧伯稽首再拜對曰：昭乎哉問也。此天地之綱紀，變化之淵源，非聖帝孰能窮其至理歟，臣雖不敏，請陳其道，令終不滅，久而不易。帝曰：願夫子推而次之，從其類序分其部主，別其宗司，昭其氣數，明其正化，可得聞乎。歧伯曰：先立其年以明其氣，金木水火土運行之數，寒暑燥濕風火臨御之化，則天道可見，民氣可調，陰陽卷舒，近而無惑，數之可數者，請遂言之。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: I know about the six transformations and six changes, the dominance and restoration of climatic influences, and the order of sweetness, bitterness, pungency, saltiness, sourness, and blandness. Regarding the transformations of the Five Periodic Cycles, sometimes they follow the Five Climatic Influences and sometimes they oppose them; at times they follow Heaven's qi but oppose Earth's qi, or vice versa. Sometimes they are harmonious with each other, and sometimes not. I have yet to fully understand these matters. I wish to understand the principles of Heaven's order, follow the patterns of Earth, harmonize their cycles, regulate their transformations, so that Heaven and Earth may act in concert without violating each other's proper roles. The ascending and descending movements of Heaven and Earth should not deviate from what is appropriate; the Five Periodic Cycles must circulate freely without contradicting their natural order. By adjusting to the correct flavors, how can one distinguish between following or opposing these influences? Qibo bowed respectfully and replied: What a clear and profound question! This is the framework of Heaven and Earth, the origin of all transformations. Who but a sage emperor could fully grasp its ultimate principles? Although I am not particularly wise, I shall explain the way so that it remains unaltered through time and endures without change. The Emperor said: I hope you, Master, will elaborate and arrange them in order. Please classify them according to their categories, distinguish their respective authorities, clarify their governing principles, reveal the nature of their qi and numbers, and explain their normal transformations. Would it be possible for me to hear this? Qibo said: First, establish the year to clarify its qi. Understand the numerical patterns of the Metal, Wood, Water, Fire, and Earth Cycles; comprehend the transformations brought by Cold, Heat, Dryness, Dampness, Wind, and Fire as they govern in turn. Then the principles of Heaven will become evident, human qi can be regulated, Yin and Yang will expand and contract clearly without confusion. As for those patterns that can be counted, I shall now explain them.

		Taiyang, Taiyu, Taiyin. Bingchen Tiandou,Bingxu Tiandou. Its movement is Cold; its transformation brings condensation, bleakness, and piercing cold; its changes manifest as snow, ice, frost, and hail; its illnesses involve intense cold remaining in the joints and valleys of the body.

		Taiyu, Taijiao,Shaozheng,Taigong,Shaoshang.

13
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
凡此太陽司天之政，氣化運行先天，天氣肅，地氣靜，寒臨太虛，陽氣不令，水土合德，上應辰星鎮星。其穀玄黅，其政肅，其令徐。寒政大舉，澤無陽燄，則火發待時。少陽中治，時雨迺涯，止極雨散，還於太陰，雲朝北極，濕化迺布，澤流萬物，寒敷于上，雷動于下，寒濕之氣，持於氣交。民病寒濕，發肌肉萎足痿不收濡寫血溢。初之氣，地氣遷，氣迺大溫，草迺早榮，民迺厲，溫病迺作，身熱頭痛嘔吐，肌腠瘡瘍。二之氣，大涼反至，民迺慘，草迺遇寒，火氣遂抑，民病氣鬱中滿，寒迺始。三之氣，天政布，寒氣行，雨迺降，民病寒，反熱中，癰疽注下，心熱瞀悶，不治者死。四之氣，風濕交爭，風化為雨，迺長迺化迺成，民病大熱，少氣肌肉萎足痿，注下赤白。五之氣，陽復化，草迺長，迺化迺成，民迺舒。終之氣，地氣正，濕令行，陰凝太虛，埃昏郊野，民迺慘悽，寒風以至，反者孕迺死。故歲宜苦以燥之溫之，必折其鬱氣，先資其化源，抑其運氣，扶其不勝，無使暴過而生其疾，食歲穀以全其真，避虛邪以安其正。適氣同異，多少制之，同寒濕者燥熱化，異寒濕者燥濕化，故同者多之，異者少之，用寒遠寒，用涼遠涼，用溫遠溫，用熱遠熱，食宜同法。有假者反常，反是者病，所謂時也。
		All these are the governing influences of Taiyang as it presides over Heaven. The qi, transformation, and movement occur in advance of their time; Heaven's qi is severe, Earth's qi is tranquil; Cold dominates the vast emptiness, while Yang qi does not prevail. Water and Earth harmonize in virtue, corresponding above to the stars Chenxing and Zhenxing. Its grains are xuan (black) and cheng (yellow); its administration is severe; its commands proceed slowly. When the Cold influence rises strongly, there is no warmth or flame of Yang in the moist environment; thus, Fire will await its time to erupt. Shaoyang governs the middle, and timely rains begin to fall. When rain reaches its extreme, it disperses and returns to Taiyin. Clouds gather toward the North Pole; Damp transformation spreads widely; moisture flows to all things. Cold prevails above, Thunder stirs below. The interplay of Cold and Damp qi lingers at the boundary where Heaven and Earth meet. The people suffer from Cold and Damp, manifesting as muscle weakness, foot paralysis with loss of control, excessive dampness causing blood overflow. First Qi: Earth's qi shifts, and the climate becomes greatly warm. Grasses flourish early; people are vulnerable to epidemics; febrile diseases arise with symptoms of body heat, headache, vomiting, and skin sores. Second Qi: A great Coolness unexpectedly arrives, causing distress among the people. Grasses encounter cold; Fire qi is suppressed. People suffer from internal stagnation and fullness in the middle jiao, marking the beginning of Cold. Third Qi: Heaven's governance is manifested, Cold qi circulates, and rain falls. People suffer from cold-related illnesses; internal Heat arises paradoxically. Boils, abscesses, and discharges occur, along with heart heat, confusion, and mental distress. If untreated, death may result. Fourth Qi: Wind and Damp contend with each other; Wind transforms into rain, promoting growth, transformation, and maturation. People suffer from intense Heat, shortness of breath, muscle atrophy, foot paralysis, and discharges of red or white mucus. Fifth Qi: Yang returns to transformation; grasses grow, transform, and mature; the people feel relief. Final Qi: Earth's qi is in order, and Damp prevails. Yin condenses into the vast emptiness; dust obscures the suburbs. The people feel sorrowful and distressed as Cold winds arrive. Those who are pregnant but have underlying imbalances may die. Therefore, for this year, bitter flavors should be used to dry and warm; one must dispel stagnated qi. First, nourish the source of transformation; restrain excessive climatic influences; support those that are insufficient. Avoid sudden extremes that could cause illness. Eat the grains appropriate to the year in order to preserve true essence, and avoid pathogenic factors by protecting normal qi. Adjust the treatment according to whether the condition is similar or different from the climatic influences. Use more or less accordingly: for conditions that are the same as Cold and Damp, employ warming and drying therapies; for those differing from Cold and Damp, use drying and damp-resolving treatments. Therefore, when the nature of the disease matches the climate, apply it in larger amounts; if different, use smaller amounts. When using Cold therapy, avoid excessive Cold; when using Cool therapy, avoid excessive Coolness; when using Warm therapy, avoid excessive Warmth; when using Heat therapy, avoid excessive Heat. The same principle applies to diet. Those who are abnormal or have false conditions will exhibit opposite responses; such deviations lead to illness—this is what is meant by the nature of time and climatic influences.

25
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
凡此陽明司天之政，氣化運行後天，天氣急，地氣明，陽專其令，炎暑大行，物燥以堅，淳風迺治，風燥橫運，流於氣交，多陽少陰，雲趨雨府，濕化迺敷。燥極而澤，其穀白丹，閒穀命太者，其耗白甲品羽，金火合德，上應太白熒惑。其政切，其令暴，蟄蟲迺見，流水不冰，民病欬嗌塞寒熱發，暴振凓癃閟，清先而勁，毛蟲迺死，熱後而暴，介蟲迺殃，其發躁勝復之作，擾而大亂，清熱之氣，持於氣交。初之氣，地氣遷，陰始凝，氣始肅，水迺冰，寒雨化，其病中熱脹面目浮腫，善眠鼽衂嚏欠嘔，小便黃赤，甚則淋。二之氣，陽迺布，民迺舒，物迺生榮，厲大至，民善暴死。三之氣，天政布，涼迺行，燥熱交合，燥極而澤，民病寒熱。四之氣，寒雨降，病暴仆，振慄譫妄，少氣嗌乾引飲及為心痛癰腫瘡瘍瘧寒之疾，骨痿血便。五之氣，春令反行，草迺生榮，民氣和。終之氣，陽氣布，候反溫，蟄蟲來見，流水不冰，民迺康平，其病溫，故食歲穀以安其氣，食間穀以去其邪，歲宜以鹹以苦以辛，汗之清之散之，安其運氣，無使受邪，折其鬱氣，資其化源。以寒熱輕重少多其制，同熱者多天化，同清者多地化，用涼遠涼，用熱遠熱，用寒遠寒，用溫遠溫，食宜同法。有假者反之，此其道也。反是者，亂天地之經，擾陰陽之紀也。
		All these are the governing principles of Yangming as it presides over Heaven. Its qi, transformation, and operation occur after their due time; Heaven's qi becomes urgent, Earth's qi clear. Yang dominates exclusively, intense Heat prevails widely, things become dry and hardened. Pure winds bring order; Dryness and Wind move laterally across the boundary of Heaven and Earth. There is more Yang than Yin; clouds gather toward the Rain Palace, and Damp transformation gradually spreads. When Dryness reaches its extreme, Damp follows. Its grains are white and red; among the interspersed grains, those designated as "Tai" (Great) consume the White Jia, Pin Yu. Metal and Fire harmonize in virtue; above they correspond to Taibai and Yinghuo stars. Its administration is sharp, its commands sudden. Hibernating insects emerge; flowing water does not freeze. People suffer from coughing, throat obstruction, alternating Cold and Heat symptoms, sudden shaking, chills, urinary retention, and constipation. Coolness arrives first with intensity, causing hair-covered creatures to die; Heat follows later in a violent manner, bringing disaster to shelled creatures. The outbreak of Dryness dominance and its subsequent restoration causes great disorder and disturbance. The interplay between Coolness and Heat lingers at the boundary of Heaven and Earth's qi. First Qi: Earth's qi shifts; Yin begins to condense, and the atmosphere turns severe. Water freezes; Cold and Rain transform. The illnesses manifest as internal Heat with distension, facial and bodily swelling, excessive sleepiness, nasal congestion, nosebleeds, sneezing, yawning, vomiting, yellow or red urine, and in severe cases, dysuria (painful urination). Second Qi: Yang spreads; the people feel relief; things begin to grow and flourish. Epidemics reach their peak, and many people die suddenly. Third Qi: Heaven's governance is active; Coolness begins to circulate. Dryness and Heat intermingle, with Dryness reaching its extreme followed by Damp. People suffer from alternating Cold and Heat symptoms. Fourth Qi: Cold and Rain descend, causing sudden collapse. Symptoms include shivering, delirium, shortness of breath, dry throat with intense thirst, chest pain, abscesses, sores, malarial chills, bone atrophy, and bloody stools. Fifth Qi: Spring's influence returns prematurely; grasses begin to grow and flourish; the people's qi is harmonious. Final Qi: Yang qi spreads; the climate unexpectedly becomes warm. Hibernating insects appear, and flowing water does not freeze; the people enjoy health and peace. However, febrile illnesses may still occur. Therefore, eat the grains of this year to stabilize one's vital energy, and consume interspersed grains to expel pathogenic influences. For this year, salty, bitter, and pungent flavors are appropriate—use them to induce sweating, clear heat, and disperse stagnation. Stabilize the climatic influence without allowing it to be invaded by external pathogens; resolve stagnant qi and nourish the source of transformation. Adjust the strength and quantity of treatments according to the severity of Cold or Heat. When conditions align with Heaven's transformation (Heavenly influence), apply more; when they align with Earth's transformation, apply less. Avoid excessive use: if using Coolness, avoid excess Coolness; if using Heat, avoid excess Heat; if using Cold, avoid excess Cold; if using Warmth, avoid excess Warmth. The same principle should be applied to diet. Those with false or abnormal conditions will respond oppositely—this is the fundamental principle. To act contrary to this would disrupt the principles of Heaven and Earth and disturb the order of Yin and Yang.

		Shaoyang, Taijiao, Jueyin, Renyin, Renshen. Its movement is Wind stirring; its transformation manifests as chaotic sounds and the breaking apart of things. Its changes bring shaking, pulling destruction, uprooting. Its illnesses include dizziness with vertigo, limb tremors, chest and 胁 (flank) pain, and startling fear.

打開字典

太羽，太角，少徵，太宮，少商。
		Taiyu , Taijiao ,Shaozheng ,Taigong ,Shaoshang .

37
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
凡此少陽司天之政，氣化運行先天，天氣正，地氣擾，風迺暴舉，木偃沙飛，炎火迺流，陰行陽化，雨迺時應，火木同德，上應熒惑歲星。其穀丹蒼，其政嚴，其令擾。故風熱參布，雲物沸騰，太陰橫流，寒迺時至，涼雨並起。民病寒中，外發瘡瘍，內為泄滿，故聖人遇之，和而不爭。往復之作，民病寒熱瘧泄，聾瞑嘔吐，上怫腫色變。初之氣，地氣遷，風勝迺搖，寒迺去，候迺大溫，草木早榮。寒來不殺，溫病迺起，其病氣怫於上，血溢目赤，欬逆頭痛血𡹌，脇滿膚腠中瘡。二之氣，火反鬱，白埃四起，雲趨雨府，風不勝濕，雨迺零，民迺康。其病熱鬱於上，欬逆嘔吐，瘡發於中，胸嗌不利，頭痛身熱，昏憒膿瘡。三之氣，天政布，炎暑至，少陽臨上，雨迺涯。民病熱中，聾瞑血溢，膿瘡欬嘔，鼽衂渴嚏欠，喉痺目赤，善暴死。四之氣，涼迺至，炎暑閒化，白露降，民氣和平，其病滿身重。五之氣，陽迺去，寒迺來，雨降，氣門迺閉，剛木早凋，民避寒邪，君子周密。終之氣，地氣正，風迺至，萬物反生，霿霧以行。其病關閉不禁，心痛，陽氣不藏而欬。抑其運氣，贊所不勝，必折其鬱氣，先取化源，暴過不生，苛疾不起。故歲宜鹹辛宜酸，滲之泄之，漬之發之，觀氣寒溫以調其過，同風熱者多寒化，異風熱者少寒化，用熱遠熱，用溫遠溫，用寒遠寒，用涼遠涼，食宜同法，此其道也。有假者反之，反是者，病之階也。
		All these are the governing characteristics of Shaoyang as it presides over Heaven; its qi, transformation and operation occur in advance. Heaven's qi is stable, while Earth's qi becomes disturbed. Wind rises suddenly with great force, trees fall down, sand flies up into the air. Fire spreads widely, Yin moves forward to support Yang transformations, and rain occurs at appropriate times. Fire and Wood harmonize in virtue; they correspond above to Yinghuo (Mars) and Suixing (Jupiter). Its grains are red and green; its administration is strict, and its commands are disruptive. Therefore, Wind and Heat spread together; clouds and phenomena surge like boiling water. Taiyin flows laterally, Cold arrives at times, Coolness and Rain arise simultaneously. The people suffer from internal Cold, with external manifestations of sores and ulcers, and internally experience diarrhea and fullness. Therefore, a sage, when encountering such conditions, harmonizes without opposing the natural order. The alternating dominance and restoration of influences cause people to suffer from alternating Cold and Heat, malaria-like symptoms with diarrhea, deafness, dim vision, vomiting, swelling in the upper body, and changes in skin color. First Qi: Earth's qi shifts; Wind prevails and stirs, Cold departs, the climate becomes greatly warm, grasses and trees flourish early. Cold arrives but does not kill; febrile diseases emerge. The illness manifests as qi stagnation in the upper part, blood overflow with red eyes, coughing against breath, headache with nosebleeds, flank fullness, and skin sores within the muscles. Second Qi: Fire becomes suppressed, white dust rises everywhere; clouds gather toward the rain palace. Wind cannot overcome Dampness; rain begins to fall, and people enjoy health. The illness manifests as Heat congestion in the upper part, coughing against breath with vomiting; sores arise internally. The chest and throat feel obstructed, headache and body fever occur, confusion arises, and abscesses form. Third Qi: Heaven's governance is active, intense Heat arrives; Shaoyang governs above, and rain begins to fall. The people suffer from internal Heat, deafness and dim vision with blood overflow; abscesses form, coughing and vomiting occur. Nasal congestion, nosebleeds, thirst, sneezing, yawns, throat obstruction, red eyes appear, and sudden death may happen. Fourth Qi: Coolness arrives; intense Heat is interrupted by transformation, white dew falls, the people's qi remains harmonious and balanced. The illnesses manifest as fullness and body heaviness. Fifth Qi: Yang departs, Cold arrives; rain falls and the gates of qi close. Strong Wood withers early; people avoid Cold pathogenic influences, while the wise remain cautious and meticulous. Final Qi: Earth's qi is in harmony; Wind arrives, causing all things to grow again unexpectedly; mist and fog move about. The illnesses manifest as inability to close the joints or control bodily functions, chest pain, failure of Yang qi to be stored, and coughing. Restraining the climatic influences and supporting those that are insufficient is essential. One must resolve stagnant qi, first nourishing the source of transformation, so that sudden extremes do not arise and severe diseases will not occur. Therefore, for this year it is appropriate to use salty, pungent, and sour flavors. Employ diuretic and purgative methods; use soaking and promoting release. Observe the Cold or Warm nature of the climate to adjust imbalances accordingly. For conditions similar to Wind-Heat, apply more Cold transformation; for those different from Wind-Heat, use less Cold transformation. Avoid excessive application: when using Heat, avoid excess heat; when using warmth, avoid excess warmth; when using Cold, avoid excess cold; when using Coolness, avoid excess coolness. Diet should follow the same principle—this is the correct method. Those with false or abnormal conditions respond oppositely; to act contrary to this principle leads to illness.

49
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
凡此太陰司天之政，氣化運行後天，陰專其政，陽氣退辟，大風時起，天氣下降，地氣上騰，原野昏霿，白埃四起，雲奔南極，寒雨數至，物成於差夏。民病寒濕腹滿身䐜憤胕腫，痞逆寒厥拘急，濕寒合德，黃黑埃昏，流行氣交，上應鎮星辰星。其政肅，其令寂，其穀黅玄。故陰凝於上，寒積於下，寒水勝火，則為冰雹，陽光不治，殺氣迺行。故有餘宜高，不及宜下，有餘宜晚，不迺宜早，土之利，氣之化也，民氣亦從之，閒穀命其太也。初之氣，地氣遷，寒迺去，春氣正，風迺來，生布萬物以榮，民氣條舒，風濕相薄，雨迺後。民病血溢，筋絡拘強，關節不利，身重筋痿。二之氣，大火正，物承化，民迺和，其病溫厲大行，遠近咸若，濕蒸相薄，雨迺時降。三之氣，天政布，濕氣降，地氣騰，雨迺時降，寒迺隨之。感於寒濕，則民病身重胕腫，胸腹滿。四之氣，畏火臨，溽蒸化，地氣騰，天氣否隔，寒風曉暮，蒸熱相薄，草木凝煙，濕化不流，則白露陰布，以成秋令。民病腠理熱，血暴溢瘧，心腹滿熱臚脹，甚則胕腫。五之氣，慘令已行，寒露下，霜迺早降，草木黃落，寒氣及體，君子周密，民病皮腠。終之氣，寒大舉，濕大化，霜迺積，陰迺凝，水堅冰，陽光不治，感於寒則病人關節禁固腰脽痛，寒濕推於氣交而為疾也。必折其鬱氣，而取化源，益其歲氣，無使邪勝，食歲穀以全其真，食閒穀以保其精。故歲宜以苦燥之溫之，甚者發之泄之。不發不泄，則濕氣外溢，肉潰皮拆而水血交流。必贊其陽火，令禦甚寒，從氣異同少多其判也，同寒者以熱化，同濕者以燥化，異者少之，同者多之，用涼遠涼，用寒遠寒，用溫遠溫，用熱遠熱，食宜同法。假者反之，此其道也，反是者病也。
		In general, the governance of Taiyin as the Heaven-Overseeing Official results in climatic and transformative forces operating after their due time. Yin dominates exclusively; Yang qi withdraws and retreats. Strong winds arise frequently. The celestial energy descends while terrestrial energy ascends. The plains become dim and obscured, with white dust rising everywhere. Clouds rush toward the southern pole, frequent cold rains fall, and all things mature unevenly during summer. The people suffer from cold-dampness, abdominal distension, body swelling and fullness, edema, stagnation with upward reversal of qi, cold extremities, rigidity, and urgency. Damp-cold combine in harmony; yellow and black dust obscure the air. Pathogenic influences circulate at the junctions of climatic forces, corresponding to the stars Zhenchen and Xingxing in the heavens above. Its governance is severe; its command is silent; its grains are yellow and black. Therefore, Yin accumulates above while Cold accumulates below; when Cold-Water overcomes Fire, hail forms. Solar warmth fails to regulate, and killing qi prevails. Therefore, when there is excess, it should be high; when insufficient, low. When in excess, it occurs late; if not so, early. The fertility of Earth and the transformation of qi determine this; the people's vital energy follows accordingly. Adjusting grain planting according to these principles ensures harmony with the Great Dao. First Qi: Terrestrial energy shifts; Cold departs. Spring qi prevails, Wind arrives. Life spreads and all things flourish; the people's vital energies extend smoothly. Wind and Dampness interact closely, followed by rain. The people suffer from blood overflow, tense and rigid sinews and meridians, impaired joint movement, heavy body sensation, and atrophy of the tendons. Second Qi: The Great Fire prevails; all things receive transformation, and the people find harmony. However, epidemic warmth spreads widely, affecting both near and far regions. Dampness rises and interacts closely, with timely rain descending. Third Qi: The celestial governance is dispersed; Dampness descends and terrestrial energy ascends, with timely rain falling. Cold follows afterward. Those affected by Cold-Dampness suffer from body heaviness, edema, and chest and abdominal distension. Fourth Qi: Fear of Fire's dominance; humidity and heat transform, terrestrial energy rises, while celestial energy becomes obstructed. Cold winds arise at dawn and dusk. Heat and dampness interact closely; grasses and trees are shrouded in mist. Damp transformation stagnates, white dew spreads under the shade, marking the arrival of autumn. The people suffer from heat in the skin pores, sudden blood overflow, malaria, fullness and heat in the chest and abdomen with distension; severe cases may develop into edema. Fifth Qi: The severe command is in effect; Cold Dew descends, frost falls early. Grasses and trees turn yellow and fall off. Cold affects the body; the virtuous remain cautious and careful. The people suffer from skin and pore disorders. Final Qi: Cold intensifies greatly, Dampness transforms extensively; frost accumulates, Yin condenses, water becomes ice. Solar warmth fails to govern. Exposure to Cold causes joint stiffness and pain in the waist and hips. Cold-Dampness converges at the junction of climatic forces and gives rise to illness. One must resolve stagnant qi and nourish the source of transformation. Strengthen the annual climatic energy to prevent pathogenic influences from prevailing. Consume grains corresponding to this year's Qi to preserve true essence, and consume other grains to safeguard vital energy. Therefore, for the year it is appropriate to employ bitter, drying, and warming methods; in severe cases, promote release or purgation. Failure to release or purge will result in Dampness overflowing externally, causing flesh ulceration, skin splitting, and the mingling of water and blood. One must support Yang and Fire to resist extreme Cold. Adjust the amount of intervention according to whether the condition aligns with or differs from the climatic influence: for those similar to Cold, use Heat transformation; for those similar to Dampness, use Dryness transformation. For differing conditions, apply less; for similar ones, apply more. Avoid excess in using Cool, Cold, Warm, and Hot therapies—diet should follow this principle as well. Those with false or abnormal patterns respond oppositely; this is the correct method. Acting contrary to it leads to illness.
