打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
凡此少陰司天之政，氣化運行先天，地氣肅，天氣明，寒交暑，熱加燥，雲馳雨府，濕化迺行，時雨迺降，金火合德，上應熒惑太白。其政明，其令切，其穀丹白。水火寒熱持於氣交而為病始也，熱病生於上，清病生於下，寒熱凌犯而爭於中，民病欬喘，血溢血泄鼽嚏目赤眥瘍，寒厥入胃心痛腰痛腹大，嗌乾腫上。初之氣，地氣遷，燥將去，寒迺始，蟄復藏，水迺冰，霜復降，風迺至，陽氣鬱，民反周密，關節禁固腰脽痛，炎暑將起，中外瘡瘍。二之氣，陽氣布，風迺行，春氣以正，萬物應榮，寒氣時至，民迺和，其病淋，目瞑目赤，氣鬱於上而熱。三之氣，天政布，大火行，庶類蕃鮮，寒氣時至，民病氣厥心痛，寒熱更作，欬喘目赤。四之氣，溽暑至，大雨時行，寒熱互至，民病寒熱，嗌乾黃癉，鼽衂飲發。五之氣，畏火臨，暑反至，陽迺化，萬物迺生迺長榮，民迺康，其病溫。終之氣，燥令行，餘火內格，腫於上，欬喘甚則血溢，寒氣數舉，則霿霧翳，病生皮腠，內舍於脇，下連少腹而作寒中地將易也。必抑其運氣，資其歲勝，折其鬱發，先取化源，無使暴過而生其病也。食歲穀以全真氣，食間穀以辟虛邪。歲宜鹹以耎之，而調其上，甚則以苦發之，以酸收之，而安其下，甚則以苦泄之。適氣同異而多少之，同天氣者以寒清化，同地氣者以溫熱化，用熱遠熱，用涼遠涼，用溫遠溫，用寒遠寒，食宜同法。有假則反，此其道也，反是者病作矣。
		In general, the governance of Shaoyin as Heaven-Overseeing Official involves climatic and transformative forces acting ahead of their due time. Terrestrial energy is severe; celestial energy is clear. Cold meets summer heat, while Heat combines with Dryness. Clouds race toward the rain domain, Damp transformation proceeds, timely rains descend. Metal and Fire harmonize in virtue, corresponding to the stars Yinghuo and Taibai above. Its governance is clear; its command is strict; its grains are red and white. Diseases arise at the junction of Water, Fire, Cold, and Heat. Heat-related illnesses originate in the upper body; cold-damp diseases manifest below. Cold and Heat invade each other and contend within the middle jiao. The people suffer from coughing, asthma, blood overflow, bloody diarrhea, nasal discharge, sneezing, red eyes, eyelid sores, Cold 厥 entering the stomach causing heart pain, lower back pain, abdominal distension, dry throat, and swelling in the upper region. First Qi: Terrestrial energy shifts, Dryness departs, and Cold begins. Insects retreat into concealment; water freezes; frost falls again; Wind arrives. Yang qi becomes stagnant. The people become cautious and reserved, suffering from joint stiffness, lower back and hip pain. Heat-summer is about to arise, causing sores both internally and externally. Second Qi: Yang qi spreads, Wind moves. Spring energy prevails; all things respond with flourishing growth. Cold occasionally arrives, and the people remain in harmony. The illnesses manifest as dysuria, eye fatigue or redness, and stagnant heat accumulating above. Third Qi: The celestial governance spreads; the Great Fire operates. All living things flourish and thrive. Cold occasionally arrives. The people suffer from qi reversal with heart pain, alternating Cold and Heat symptoms, coughing, asthma, and red eyes. Fourth Qi: Humid summer heat arrives, with heavy rains occurring at times. Cold and Heat alternate; the people suffer from alternating Cold-Heat symptoms, dry throat, yellow rashes, nasal discharge, nosebleeds, and fluid-related disorders. Fifth Qi: Fear of Fire's dominance, but summer heat unexpectedly returns. Yang transforms; all things grow and flourish, the people enjoy health, yet diseases of warmth still occur. Final Qi: Dryness prevails; residual Fire is internally obstructed, causing swelling above. Coughing and asthma intensify to the point of blood overflow. Frequent Cold attacks bring fog-like obscurity. Illnesses arise in the skin and pores, internally affecting the flanks, extending downward to the lower abdomen, resulting in internal Cold. The terrestrial conditions are about to change. One must restrain the climatic influences, support the annual dominance, resolve stagnant and eruptive conditions, first nourish the source of transformation; do not allow sudden extremes to arise and cause illness. Consume grains corresponding to this year's Qi to preserve true energy, and consume other grains to ward off pathogenic influences. For this year, salty flavors should be used to soften and regulate the upper body. In severe cases, bitter flavors can promote release, while sour ones help 收敛 (gather) and stabilize the lower region; in extreme conditions, bitter herbs may also be employed for purgation. Adjust the amount according to whether conditions align with or differ from climatic influences. For those similar to celestial energy, use Cold and Clearing methods; for those aligned with terrestrial energy, employ Warming and Heat transformation. Avoid excess: when using Heat, avoid excessive heat; when using Coolness, excessive coolness; when using Warmth, excessive warmth; when using Cold, excessive cold. Diet should follow this same principle. If there is a false or abnormal pattern, the approach must be reversed—this is the correct principle. Acting contrary to it will lead to illness.

顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
凡此厥陰司天之政，氣化運行後天，諸同正歲，氣化運行同天，天氣擾，地氣正，風生高遠，炎熱從之，雲趨雨府，濕化迺行，風火同德，上應歲星熒惑。其政撓，其令速，其穀蒼丹，閒穀言太者，其耗文角品羽。風燥火熱，勝復更作，蟄蟲來見，流水不冰，熱病行於下，風病行於上，風燥勝復形於中。初之氣，寒始肅，殺氣方至，民病寒於右之下。二之氣，寒不去，華雪水冰，殺氣施化霜迺降，名草上焦，寒雨數至，陽復化，民病熱於中。三之氣，天政布，風迺時舉，民病泣出耳鳴掉眩。四之氣，溽暑濕熱相薄爭於左之上，民病黃癉而為胕腫。五之氣，燥濕更勝，沈陰迺布，寒氣及體，風雨迺行。終之氣，畏火司令，陽迺大化，蟄蟲出見，流水不冰，地氣大發，草生，人迺舒，其病溫厲，必折其鬱氣，資其化源，贊其運氣，無使邪勝，歲宜以辛調上，以鹹調下，畏火之氣無妄犯之，用溫遠溫，用熱遠熱，用涼遠涼，用寒遠寒，食宜同法。有假反常，此之道也，反是者病。
		In general, the governance of Jueyin as Heaven-Overseeing Officer involves climatic and transformative influences operating after their due time. In all cases corresponding to standard years, these forces align with celestial patterns. Celestial energy becomes turbulent while terrestrial energy remains stable; Wind arises from high altitudes, followed by heat. Clouds gather toward the rain domain; Damp transformation proceeds. Wind and Fire harmonize in virtue; they correspond above to the stars Suixing and Yinghuo. Its governance is turbulent; its command swift; its grains are green and red. The term "Tai" in reference to other grains indicates the consumption of Wenjiao, Pinju, and similar categories. Wind, Dryness, Fire, and Heat alternate in dominance and restoration. Insects that should hibernate appear; flowing water does not freeze. Heat-related diseases affect the lower body, Wind disorders afflict the upper region, while the middle is influenced by alternating Wind-Dryness dominance. First Qi: Cold begins to prevail; killing qi arrives. The people suffer from Cold affecting the lower right side of their bodies. Second Qi: Cold does not depart; flowers and snow turn to ice. Killing qi spreads, transforming into frost that falls. Grasses wither at the top; frequent cold rains descend. Yang energy returns and transforms, causing internal heat in the people. Third Qi: The celestial governance spreads; Wind arises at its proper time. The people suffer from weeping, ear ringing, dizziness, and vertigo. Fourth Qi: Humid summer heat and Damp-Heat interact and contend in the upper left region. The people suffer from yellow rashes and edema. Fifth Qi: Dryness and Dampness alternate in dominance; deep Yin spreads, Cold affects the body, and Wind-Rain occur. Final Qi: Fear of Fire's command; Yang energy transforms greatly. Insects that hibernate emerge, flowing water does not freeze, terrestrial energy strongly manifests, grasses grow, and people feel relaxed. The diseases involve epidemic warmth. One must resolve stagnant qi and support the source of transformation, assist climatic influences, to prevent pathogenic forces from prevailing. For this year, use pungent flavors to regulate the upper region and salty ones for the lower; avoid violating Fire's energy arbitrarily. Avoid excess: when using Warmth, avoid excessive warmth; when using Heat, excessive heat; when using Coolness or Cold, their extremes as well. Diet should follow these same principles. Those with abnormal or false patterns respond oppositely—this is the principle. Acting contrary to this leads to illness.

74
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
帝曰：善。夫子言可謂悉矣。然何以明其應乎。歧伯曰：昭乎哉問也。夫六氣者，行有次，止有位，故常以正月朔日平旦視之，覩其位而知其所在矣。運有餘，其至先，運不及，其至後，此天之道，氣之常也。運非有餘非不足，是謂正歲，其至當其時也。帝曰：勝復之氣，其常在也，災眚時至候也，柰何。歧伯曰：非氣化者，是謂災也。
		The Emperor said: Well spoken. Your words may be considered complete and thorough. Yet, how can we clarify their corresponding effects? Qibo said: What a clear and insightful question! The Six Climatic Influences have an ordered sequence in their movement and fixed positions when they cease. Therefore, on the first day of the first lunar month at dawn, one observes them; by discerning their positions, one can know where each resides. When the movement is excessive, its arrival occurs early; when insufficient, it arrives late. This is the Dao of Heaven and the normal pattern of qi. When movement is neither excessive nor deficient, this is called a standard year; its arrival coincides with the proper time. The Emperor asked: The dominance and restoration of climatic forces are constant, yet disasters and misfortunes occur at times. How should one understand this? Qibo said: When the transformation of qi is abnormal, it is called a disaster.

75
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
帝曰：天地之數終始柰何。歧伯曰：悉乎哉問也。是明道也。數之始，起於上而終於下，歲半之前，天氣主之，歲半之後，地氣主之，上下交互，氣交主之，歲紀畢矣。故曰位明氣月可知乎，所謂氣也。
		The Emperor asked: How do the numbers governing the beginning and end of Heaven and Earth function? Qibo said: What a thorough question! This is the essence of understanding the Dao. The sequence begins from above and ends below. Before the halfway point of the year, Heaven's qi dominates; after that midpoint, Earth's qi takes over. When upper and lower forces interact, it is the Interacting Qi (Qi Jiao) that governs them—thus completing the annual cycle. Therefore, one may say: When positions are clear, can the months' qi be known? This is what is meant by "qi."

76
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
帝曰：余司其事，則而行之，不合其數，何也。歧伯曰：氣用有多少，化洽有盛衰，衰盛多少，同其化也。帝曰：願聞同化何如。歧伯曰：風溫春化同，熱曛昏火夏化同，勝與復同，燥清煙露秋化同，雲雨昏暝埃長夏化同，寒氣霜雪冰冬化同，此天地五運六氣之化，更用盛衰之常也。帝曰：五運行同天化者，命曰天符，余知之矣。願聞同地化者，何謂也。歧伯曰：太過而同天化者三，不及而同天化者亦三，太過而同地化者三，不及而同地化者亦三，此凡二十四歲也。帝曰：願聞其所謂也。歧伯曰：甲辰甲戌太宮，下加太陰，壬寅壬申太角，下加厥陰，庚子庚午太商，下加陽明，如是者三。癸巳癸亥少徵，下加少陽，辛丑辛未少羽，下加太陽，癸卯癸酉少徵，下加少陰，如是者三。戊子戊午太徵，上臨少陰，戊寅戊申太徵，上臨少陽，丙辰丙戌太羽，上臨太陽，如是者三。丁巳丁亥少角，上臨厥陰，乙卯乙酉少商，上臨陽明，己丑己未少宮，上臨太陰，如是者三。除此二十四歲，則不加不臨也。帝曰：加者何謂。歧伯曰：太過而加同天符，不及而加同歲會也。帝曰：臨者何謂。歧伯曰：太過不及，皆曰天符，而變行有多少，病形有微甚，生死有早晏耳。
		The Emperor asked: I oversee these matters and follow their principles, yet they do not align with the numbers. Why is this so? Qibo said: The application of qi varies in quantity; transformation and harmony fluctuate between prosperity and decline. Prosperity, decline, abundance, and scarcity all belong to the same process of transformation. The Emperor asked: I would like to hear how this "same transformation" manifests. Qibo said: Wind and warmth correspond to spring transformation; heat, haze, and dusk-fire correspond to summer transformation. Dominance and restoration are the same. Dryness and clarity, smoke and dew correspond to autumn transformation; clouds, rain, dusk, and dust correspond to late summer (long shu) transformation; cold, frost, snow, and ice correspond to winter transformation. These are the transformations of Heaven's Five Movements and Earth's Six Climatic Influences—their alternating use and fluctuating prosperity and decline form a constant pattern. The Emperor said: When the Five Movements align with Heaven's transformation, it is called Tianfu (Heavenly Accord), and I understand this. I would like to know what is meant by those that align with Earth's transformation. Qibo said: There are three instances of excessive movement aligning with Heaven's transformation and also three of insufficient movement aligning similarly. Likewise, there are three cases of excessive movement aligning with Earth's transformation and another three for the insufficient. This totals twenty-four years. The Emperor said: I would like to hear what these refer to specifically. Qibo said: Jiachen and Jiayou are Tai Gong, with Taiyin added below; Renyin and Ren Shen are Taijiao, with Juyin added below; Gengzi and Gengwu are Taishang, with Yangming added below. These constitute three instances. łoż Kuisi and Kuihai are Shaozheng, with Shaoyang added below; Xinchou and Xinwei are Shaoyu, with Taiyang added below; Kuimao and Kuizhou are Shaozheng again, with Shaoyin added below. These also constitute three instances. Wuzi and Wumu are Taizheng, with Shaoyin above; Wuyin and Wushen are also Taizheng, but with Shaoyang above; Bingchen and Bingyou are Taiyu, with Taiyang above. These form three instances. Dingsi and Dinghai are Shaojiao, with Jueyin above; Yimao and Yiyou are Shaoshang, with Yangming above; Jiouchou and Jiuni are Shaogong, with Taiyin above. These also form three instances. Beyond these twenty-four years, there is neither addition below nor presence above. The Emperor asked: What does "addition" mean? Qibo said: When excessive, the addition aligns with Tianfu (Heavenly Concord); when insufficient, it aligns with Suikui (Annual Accord). The Emperor asked: What does "presence above" mean? Qibo said: Whether excessive or insufficient, they are all called Tianfu (Heaven's Accord). However, the changes vary in degree; disease manifestations differ in mildness and severity; and outcomes of life and death occur at different times.

77
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
帝曰：夫子言用寒遠寒，用熱遠熱，余未知其然也，願聞何謂遠。歧伯曰：熱無犯熱，寒無犯寒，從者和，逆者病，不可不敬畏而遠之，所謂時興六位也。帝曰：溫涼何如。歧伯曰：司氣以熱，用熱無犯，司氣以寒，用寒無犯，司氣以涼，用涼無犯，司氣以溫，用溫無犯，閒氣同其主無犯，異其主則小犯之，是謂四畏，必謹察之。帝曰：善。其犯者何如。歧伯曰：天氣反時，則可依則，及勝其主則可犯，以平為期，而不可過，是謂邪氣反勝者。故曰：無失天信，無逆氣宜，無翼其勝，無贊其復，是謂至治。
		The Emperor asked: You said, "When using cold therapy, avoid cold; when using heat therapy, avoid heat." I do not yet understand this. Please explain what you mean by "avoiding" (yuan). Qibo said: When it is hot, do not violate the nature of heat; when it is cold, do not contravene the nature of cold. Following this principle brings harmony; going against it causes illness. One cannot fail to revere and avoid these forces. This is what is meant by the six positions according to seasonal timing. The Emperor asked: What about warmth and coolness? Qibo said: When the governing qi is heat, using heat should not violate it; when the governing qi is cold, using cold should not contravene it. When the governing qi is coolness, using coolness must also align with it; and when the governing qi is warmth, using warmth must follow suit. For intervening (or transitional) qi that shares the same ruler as the main climatic force, do not violate it either. If its ruler differs from the main force, then a minor violation may occur. These four principles are called the "Four Fears," and one must carefully observe them. The Emperor said: Well spoken. What are the consequences of violating these principles? Qibo said: When Heaven's qi acts contrary to the season, one may still follow established principles. If it overcomes its ruler, then a violation is permissible, but only with the aim of restoring balance and without excess. This is called an abnormal climatic force overpowering normally dominant influences. Therefore, it is said: Do not lose faith in Heaven's timing; do not oppose the appropriate qi; do not assist excessive dominance; do not support restoration beyond need. This is called supreme regulation and harmony.

		The Emperor said: Well spoken. The Emperor asked: The Five Movements and Climatic Influences govern the annual cycle—do they follow fixed numerical patterns? Qibo said: I shall explain them in order.

Heat transformation: 2; rain transformation: 5; dryness transformation: 4—these are called standard transformation days. 		All these fixed regulations of climatic cycles, including dominance, restoration, and standard transformations, have constant numerical patterns; they must not be overlooked. Therefore, those who understand the essentials can summarize everything in one sentence; those who do not grasp these essentials will find their knowledge scattered and endless—this is what it means.

140
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
帝曰：善。五運之氣，亦復歲乎。歧伯曰：鬱極迺發，待時而作也。帝曰：請問其所謂也。歧伯曰：五常之氣，太過不及，其發異也。帝曰：願卒聞之。歧伯曰：太過者暴，不及者徐，暴者為病甚，徐者為病持。帝曰：太過不及，其數何如。歧伯曰：太過者其數成，不及者其數生，土常以生也。
		The Emperor said: Well said. Are the Five Movements' qi also repeated annually? Qibo said: When stagnation reaches its extreme, it will erupt; it waits for the right time to act. The Emperor asked: Please explain what you mean by that. Qibo said: The Five Regular Qi, when excessive or insufficient, will manifest as abnormal phenomena. The Emperor said: I wish to hear the full explanation. Qibo said: When qi is excessive, it acts suddenly; when insufficient, it progresses slowly. Sudden action causes severe illness, while slow progression results in lingering disease. The Emperor asked: What are the numerical values for excess and deficiency? Qibo said: When qi is excessive, its number completes; when insufficient, its number generates. Earth always follows the principle of generation.

141
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
帝曰：其發也何如。歧伯曰：土鬱之發，巖谷震驚，雷殷氣交，埃昏黃黑，化為白氣，飄驟高深，擊石飛空，洪水迺從，川流漫衍，田牧土駒。化氣迺敷，善為時雨，始生始長，始化始成。故民病心腹脹，腸鳴而為數後，甚則心痛脇䐜，嘔吐霍亂，飲發注下，胕腫身重。雲奔雨府，霞擁朝陽，山澤埃昏。其迺發也，以其四氣。雲橫天山，浮游生滅，怫之先兆。金鬱之發，天潔地明，風清氣切，大涼迺舉，草樹浮煙，燥氣以行，霿霧數起，殺氣來至，草木蒼乾，金迺有聲。故民病欬逆，心脇滿，引少腹善暴痛，不可反側，嗌乾面塵色惡。出澤焦枯，土凝霜鹵，怫迺發也，其氣五。夜零白露，林莽聲悽，怫之兆也。水鬱之發，陽氣迺辟，陰氣暴舉，大寒迺至，川澤嚴凝，寒雰結為霜雪，甚則黃黑昏翳，流行氣交，迺為霜殺，水迺見祥。故民病寒客心痛，腰脽痛，大關節不利，屈伸不便，善厥逆，痞堅腹滿。陽光不治，空積沈陰，白埃昏暝，而迺發也，其氣二火前後。太虛深玄，氣猶麻散，微見而隱，色黑微黃，怫之先兆也。木鬱之發，太虛埃昏，雲物以擾，大風迺至，屋發折木，木有變。故民病胃脘當心而痛，上支兩脇，鬲咽不通，食飲不下，甚則耳鳴眩轉，目不識人，善暴僵仆。太虛蒼埃，天山一色，或氣濁色黃黑，鬱若橫雲不起雨而迺發也，其氣無常。長川草偃，柔葉呈陰，松吟高山，虎嘯巖岫，怫之先兆也。火鬱之發，太虛腫翳，大明不彰，炎火行，大暑至，山澤燔燎，材木流津，廣廈騰煙，土浮霜鹵，止水迺減，蔓草焦黃，風行惑言，濕化迺後。故民病少氣瘡瘍癰腫，脇腹胸背，面首四支，䐜憤臚脹，瘍疿嘔逆，瘛瘲骨痛，節迺有動，注下溫瘧，腹中暴痛，血溢流注，精液迺少，目赤心熱，甚則瞀悶懊憹，善暴死。刻終大溫，汗濡玄府，其迺發也，其氣四。動復則靜，陽極反陰，濕令迺化迺成。華發水凝，山川冰雪焰陽午澤，怫之先兆也。有怫之應而後報也，皆觀其極而迺發也，木發無時，水隨火也。謹候其時，病可與期，失時反歲，五氣不行，生化收藏，政無恒也。
		The Emperor asked: What are their manifestations? Qibo said: When Earth Qi becomes stagnant and erupts, it causes mountain valleys to tremble with shock; thunder rumbles as the atmosphere intermingles. Dust and haze turn yellow or black, transforming into white qi that drifts swiftly at great heights and depths, striking stones and flying through the air. Floodwaters follow, rivers overflow and spread widely, fields and pastures are submerged, and even the earth's young creatures (such as foals) suffer. The transforming qi then spreads; it brings timely rain that initiates growth, promotes development, and facilitates transformation and completion. Therefore, the people suffer from abdominal distension and bloating, intestinal rumbling with frequent diarrhea; in severe cases, they experience chest pain, flank fullness, vomiting, dysentery, excessive thirst, watery discharge, edema, and heavy body sensation. Clouds rush toward the rain domain; rosy clouds gather before the morning sun; mountains and marshes are shrouded in dust and haze. Its eruption occurs according to its four qi patterns. Clouds stretch across the sky and mountains, drifting and vanishing; this is an early omen of disturbance. When Metal Qi becomes stagnant and erupts, the sky is clear and the earth bright; winds are crisp and invigorating. A great coolness arises; grasses and trees appear shrouded in drifting smoke as dry qi spreads. Dense mists frequently arise, killing qi approaches, vegetation turns withered and gray, and a metallic sound can be heard. Therefore, the people suffer from coughing with upward qi reversal, fullness in the heart and flanks, sudden pain drawing up to the lower abdomen that cannot be relieved by turning or lying on one's side; they also experience dry throat and a dusty complexion. Marshes become parched and withered, the earth hardens as frost and salt accumulate—this is when stagnation erupts. Its qi pattern involves five phases. White dew falls at night; the forest sounds mournful—this is an omen of disturbance. When Water Qi becomes stagnant and erupts, yang qi withdraws while yin qi suddenly rises; great cold arrives. Rivers and marshes freeze solidly, cold mists condense into frost and snow. In severe cases, yellow or black haze obscures the sky, moving qi intermingles, forming a killing frost—this is when water reveals its omen. Therefore, the people suffer from cold invading the heart causing pain, lower back and hip pain, stiffness in major joints, difficulty bending or stretching, frequent reversal of qi (such as cold extremities), and abdominal fullness with obstruction. When yang light fails to prevail, empty accumulations of deep yin remain; white dust and dimness obscure the sky—this is when it erupts. Its qi follows a pattern with two fire phases preceding or following. The great void is deep and mysterious; the qi disperses like flax fibers, faintly visible yet hidden. Its color is black with a slight tinge of yellow—this is an early omen of stagnation. When Wood Qi becomes stagnant and erupts, dust fills the great void; clouds and objects are disturbed, and a great wind arrives. Roofs are torn off, trees uprooted or broken—wood undergoes transformation. Therefore, the people suffer from pain in the epigastrium centered on the heart, with upward pressure extending to both flanks; the diaphragm and throat become obstructed, preventing food or drink from going down. In severe cases, tinnitus and dizziness occur, vision becomes blurred so that they cannot recognize people, and sudden collapse may happen. The great void turns gray with dust; the sky and mountains appear of one color, or the qi is murky in yellow or black. Stagnation lingers like a horizontal cloud that does not bring rain—this is when it erupts, its qi pattern being irregular. Long rivers see grasses bent low; tender leaves display yin characteristics; pines whisper on high mountains, and tigers roar from rocky hills—these are omens of impending disturbance. When Fire Qi becomes stagnant and erupts, a swelling haze fills the great void; the bright sun is obscured. Fiery heat spreads, intense summer heat arrives. Mountains and marshes blaze with fire; trees exude sap, grand buildings emit smoke, soil surfaces with frost and salt deposits, still water levels decrease, creeping grass turns scorched yellow, winds carry confusing words, and damp transformation follows later. Therefore, the people suffer from weak breath and abscesses with swelling; pain occurs in the flanks, abdomen, chest, back, face, head, and limbs. Fullness and distension arise, sores appear, vomiting and upward reversal occur, convulsions and bone pain manifest, joints become affected, dysentery and feverish malaria develop, sudden abdominal pain strikes, blood overflows and flows abnormally, bodily fluids diminish, eyes redden and the heart feels hot; in severe cases, confusion, mental distress, and sudden death may occur. When the final moment of intense heat arrives, sweat moistens the dark palaces—this is when it erupts; its qi pattern follows four phases. When movement returns to stillness, yang reaching its extreme turns into yin—damp transformation then occurs and completes. Flowers bloom with water condensation; mountains and rivers are covered in ice, snow, and fiery midday heat over marshes—this is an omen of impending disturbance. There must be a response to stagnation before it manifests; all phenomena reach their extremes and then erupt. Wood-related eruptions occur without fixed timing, while water follows fire. Carefully observe the proper time; illness can then be anticipated. If timing is missed and seasons are reversed, the Five Qi will not function properly, disrupting growth, transformation, storage, and gathering—governing principles become unstable.

142
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
帝曰：水發而雹雪，土發而飄驟，木發而毀折，金發而清明，火發而曛昧，何氣使然。歧伯曰：氣有多少，發有微甚，微者當其氣，甚者兼其下，徵其下氣而見可知也。
		The Emperor asked: When water erupts, hail and snow fall; when earth erupts, drifting rain occurs suddenly; when wood erupts, destruction and breaking follow; when metal erupts, clarity and brightness arise; when fire erupts, dimness and obscurity occur. What qi causes these phenomena? Qibo said: The amount of qi varies, and its eruption ranges from subtle to severe. Subtle eruptions correspond only to their own element; severe ones also involve the next lower element. By observing the characteristics of the lower element, one can understand what is happening.

143
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
帝曰：善。五氣之發，不當位者何也。歧伯曰：命其差。帝曰：差有數乎。歧伯曰：後皆三十度而有奇也。帝曰：氣至而先後者何。歧伯曰：運太過則其至先，運不及則其至後，此候之常也。帝曰：當時而至者何也。歧伯曰：非太過，非不及，則至當時，非是者眚也。帝曰：善。氣有非時而化者何也。歧伯曰：太過者，當其時，不及者，歸其己勝也。帝曰：四時之氣，至有早晏高下左右，其候何如。歧伯曰：行有逆順，至有遲速，故太過者化先天，不及者化後天。帝曰：願聞其行，何謂也。歧伯曰：春氣西行，夏氣北行，秋氣東行，冬氣南行。故春氣始於下，秋氣始於上，夏氣始於中，冬氣始於標，春氣始於左，秋氣始於右，冬氣始於後，夏氣始於前，此四時正化之常。故至高之地，冬氣常在，至下之地，春氣常在，必謹察之。帝曰：善。
		The Emperor said: Well said. Five Qi eruptions, what does it mean when they occur out of their proper positions? Qibo said: It is called a deviation in the mandate. The Emperor asked: Is there a numerical pattern to these deviations? Qibo said: Each deviation is approximately thirty degrees and more. The Emperor asked: What determines whether the arrival of qi occurs earlier or later? Qibo said: When the movement is excessive, its arrival comes early; when it is insufficient, its arrival is delayed. This is a regular pattern of observation. The Emperor asked: What does it mean when qi arrives at the proper time? Qibo said: When neither excessive nor insufficient, the arrival occurs exactly on schedule. Any deviation from this is a disorder or abnormality. The Emperor said: Well said. What does it mean when qi transforms outside of its proper time? Qibo said: When excessive, the qi dominates during its own season; when insufficient, it is subdued by another element that naturally overcomes it. The Emperor asked: The qi of the four seasons arrives early or late, rises or falls, appears on the left or right—what are their signs? Qibo said: The movement of qi has directions, either contrary or in harmony; its arrival varies in speed. Therefore, when excessive, transformation occurs before the proper time; when insufficient, it occurs after. The Emperor asked: I wish to hear about these movements—what do you mean? Qibo said: Spring qi moves westward, summer qi northward, autumn qi eastward, and winter qi southward. Therefore, spring qi begins from below, autumn qi from above; summer qi originates in the center, and winter qi at the surface. Spring qi starts on the left, autumn on the right, winter at the back, and summer in front—this is the regular pattern of seasonal transformation. Therefore, in the highest regions, winter qi remains constantly; in the lowest regions, spring qi is ever-present. One must carefully observe these patterns. The Emperor said: Well said.

144
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
黃帝問曰：五運六氣之應見六化之正，六變之紀，何如。歧伯對曰：夫六氣正紀，有化有變，有勝有復，有用有病，不同其候，帝欲何乎。帝曰：願盡聞之。歧伯曰：請遂言之。夫氣之所至也，厥陰所至為和平，少陰所至為暄，太陰所至為埃溽，少陽所至為炎暑，陽明所至為清勁，太陽所至為寒雰，時化之常也。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: How do the Five Movements and Six Qi correspond to the six regular transformations and the six changes? Qibo replied: The Six Qi follow regular patterns, involving transformation and change, dominance and restoration, utility and disease. Their signs differ; what would Your Majesty like to know? The Emperor said: I wish to hear it in full. Qibo said: Please allow me to explain fully. When the qi arrives, what Juyin brings is harmony and peace; what Shaoyin brings is warmth. What Taiyin brings is dust and humidity; what Shaoyang brings is heat and summer. What Yangming brings is clarity and vigor; what Taiyang brings is cold mist—these are the regular seasonal transformations.

145
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
厥陰所至為風府，為璺啟。少陰所至為火府，為舒榮。太陰所至為雨府，為員盈。少陽所至為熱府，為行出。陽明所至為司殺府，為庚蒼。太陽所至為寒府，為歸藏。司化之常也。
		Juyin's arrival brings wind domain, and causes cracks to open. Shaoyin's arrival brings the fire domain, promoting expansion and flourishing. Taiyin's arrival brings the rain domain, resulting in fullness and abundance. Shaoyang's arrival brings the heat domain, promoting movement and outward expansion. Yangming's arrival brings the killing domain, resulting in dryness and withering. Taiyang's arrival brings the cold domain, leading to return and storage. This is the regular pattern of governing transformation.

厥陰所至為生，為風搖。少陰所至為榮，為形見。太陰所至為化，為雲雨。少陽所至為長，為蕃鮮。陽明所至為收，為霧露。太陽所至為藏，為周密。氣化之常也。

Shaoyang's arrival promotes growth, resulting in abundant freshness. Yangming's arrival brings harvest, manifesting as mist and dew. Taiyang's arrival brings storage, resulting in thoroughness and compactness. This is the regular pattern of qi transformation.

	六元正紀... :	
厥陰所至為風生，終為肅。少陰所至為熱生，中為寒。太陰所至為濕生，終為注雨。少陽所至為火生，終為蒸溽。陽明所至為燥生，終為涼。太陽所至為寒生，中為溫。德化之常也。厥陰所至為毛化，少陰所至為羽化，太陰所至為倮化，少陽所至為羽化，陽明所至為介化，太陽所至為鱗化，德化之常也。
		Juyin's arrival brings wind and life, ultimately leading to severity. Shaoyin's arrival brings heat and vitality, with cold in the middle. Taiyin's arrival generates dampness, ultimately resulting in heavy rain. Shaoyang's arrival brings fire and vitality, ultimately leading to steaming humidity. Yangming's arrival generates dryness, ultimately resulting in coolness. Taiyang's arrival brings cold and vitality, with warmth in the middle. This is the regular pattern of virtue-based transformation. Juyin's arrival promotes hairy transformation; Shaoyin promotes feathered transformation; Taiyin promotes hairless (naked) transformation. Shaoyang also brings feathered transformation, Yangming promotes shelled transformation, and Taiyang promotes scaled transformation—this is the regular pattern of virtue-based change.

		Juyin's arrival brings birth transformation; Shaoyin, flourishing transformation; Taiyin, moistening transformation. Shaoyang promotes lush growth transformation; Yangming, firmness transformation; and Taiyang, storage transformation—this is the regular mode of governance.

	六元正紀... :	
厥陰所至為飄怒太涼，少陰所至為大暄寒，太陰所至為雷霆驟注烈風，少陽所至為飄風燔燎霜凝，陽明所至為散落溫，太陽所至為寒雪冰雹白埃，氣變之常也。
		Juyin's arrival brings drifting winds and excessive coolness; Shaoyin's, great warmth followed by cold. Taiyin's brings thunder, lightning, sudden heavy rain, and fierce winds. Shaoyang's brings drifting winds, blazing fires, frost condensation. Yangming's brings scattering and mild warmth; Taiyang's brings cold snow, ice, hail, and white dust—this is the regular pattern of climatic change.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
厥陰所至為撓動，為迎隨。少陰所至為高明焰，為曛。太陰所至為沈陰，為白埃，為晦暝。少陽所至為光顯，為彤雲，為曛。陽明所至為煙埃，為霜，為勁切，為悽鳴。太陽所至為剛固，為堅芒為立，令行之常也。
		Juyin's arrival causes disturbance and movement, manifesting as resistance or compliance. Shaoyin's arrival produces lofty brightness and flame, resulting in dimness. Taiyin's arrival results in deep yin, white dust, and darkness. Shaoyang's arrival produces brightness and visibility, manifesting as red clouds and dimness. Yangming's arrival results in smoke and dust, frost, sharp winds, and mournful sounds. Taiyang's arrival brings rigidity and firmness, with sharp edges and stability—this is the regular way of issuing commands.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
厥陰所至為裏急，少陰所至為瘍胗身熱，太陰所至為積飲否隔，少陽所至為嚏嘔為瘡瘍，陽明所至為浮虛，太陽所至為屈伸不利，病之常也。厥陰所至為支痛，少陰所至為驚惑惡寒戰慄譫妄，太陰所至為蓄滿，少陽所至為驚躁瞀味暴病，陽明所至為鼽尻陰股膝髀腨䯒足病，太陽所至為腰痛，病之常也。厥陰所至為緛戾，少陰所至為悲妄衂衊，太陰所至為中滿霍亂吐下，少陽所至為喉痺耳鳴嘔涌，陽明所至皴揭，太陽所至為寢汗痙，病之常也。厥陰所至為脇痛嘔泄，少陰所至為語笑，太陰所至為重胕腫，少陽所至為暴注瞤瘛暴死，陽明所至為鼽嚏，太陽所至為流泄禁止，病之常也。
		Juyin's arrival causes internal urgency; Shaoyin results in sores, swellings, and body heat. Taiyin leads to fluid accumulation, obstruction, and blockage of the digestive tract. Shaoyang causes sneezing, vomiting, and abscesses. Yangming produces floating weakness; Taiyang results in difficulty bending or stretching—these are regular patterns of disease. Juyin's arrival results in limb pain; Shaoyin causes startlement, confusion, aversion to cold, chills, and delirium. Taiyin produces retention and fullness; Shaoyang leads to sudden agitation, mental confusion, and abrupt illness. Yangming affects the nose, perineum, thighs, knees, hips, calves, shins, and feet with disease; Taiyang causes lower back pain—these are regular patterns of illness. Juyin's arrival leads to twisting and distortion; Shaoyin produces sorrow, delusion, nosebleeds, and bloodshot eyes. Taiyin causes central fullness, dysentery with vomiting and diarrhea; Shaoyang results in throat obstruction, tinnitus, and vomiting. Yangming brings dry skin and peeling; Taiyang leads to night sweats and spasm—these are regular patterns of sickness. Juyin's arrival causes flank pain, vomiting, and diarrhea; Shaoyin leads to talking and laughing. Taiyin results in heaviness and edema; Shaoyang produces sudden dysentery, twitching convulsions, and sudden death. Yangming causes nasal discharge and sneezing; Taiyang brings uncontrollable urination or its complete cessation—these are regular patterns of diseases.

152
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
凡此十二變者，報德以德，報化以化，報政以政，報令以令，氣高則高，氣下則下，氣後則後，氣前則前，氣中則中，氣外則外，位之常也。故風勝則動，熱勝則腫，燥勝則乾，寒勝則浮，濕勝則濡泄，甚則水閉胕腫，隨氣所在以言其變耳。
		All these twelve changes follow the principle: virtue responds with virtue, transformation with transformation, governance with governance, and command with command. When qi is high, it manifests as high; when low, as low. When delayed, it appears late; when early, it arrives ahead of time. When central, it affects the center; when external, it influences the exterior—this is the regular positioning pattern. Therefore, when wind dominates, movement occurs; when heat dominates, swelling results. When dryness dominates, dryness follows; when cold dominates, floating symptoms arise. When dampness dominates, moist diarrhea ensues; in severe cases, water retention and edema occur. These changes depend on where the qi resides.

153
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
帝曰：願聞其用也。歧伯曰：夫六氣之用各歸不勝而為化。故太陰雨化，施於太陽，太陽寒化，施於少陰，少陰熱化，施於陽明，陽明燥化，施於厥陰，厥陰風化，施於太陰，各命其所在以徵之也。帝曰：自得其位何如。歧伯曰：自得其位，常化也。帝曰：願聞所在也。歧伯曰：命其位而方，月可知也。帝曰：六位之氣盈虛何如。歧伯曰：太少異也，太者之至徐而常，少者暴而亡。帝曰：天地之氣，盈虛何如。歧伯曰：天氣不足，地氣隨之，地氣不足，天氣從之，運居其中而常先也。惡所不勝，歸所同和，隨運歸從而生其病也。故上勝則天氣降而下，下勝則地氣遷而上，多少而差其分，微者小差，甚者大差，甚則位易氣交易，則大變生而病作矣。大要曰：甚紀五分，微紀七分，其差可見，此之謂也。
		The Emperor said: I wish to hear about their applications. Qibo said: The Six Qi each transform by acting upon what they do not overcome. Therefore, Taiyin's rain transformation acts upon Taiyang; Taiyang's cold transformation acts upon Shaoyin. Shaoyin's heat transformation acts upon Yangming; Yangming's dryness transformation acts upon Juyin. Juyin's wind transformation acts upon Taiyin—each is named according to its location and verified accordingly. The Emperor asked: What happens when each qi remains in its proper position? Qibo said: When each occupies its proper place, it follows the regular pattern of transformation. The Emperor asked: I wish to hear about where they reside. Qibo said: Naming their positions according to direction, the months can be known. The Emperor asked: How do the Six Qi's waxing and waning manifest? Qibo said: Tai and Shao differ. When Tai arrives, it is slow but regular; when Shao arrives, it is sudden yet fleeting. The Emperor asked: How do the qi of Heaven and Earth wax and wane? Qibo said: When Heavenly Qi is insufficient, Earthly Qi follows it; when Earthly Qi is insufficient, Heavenly Qi follows. The movement resides between them and usually leads first. They resent what they cannot overcome, return to what harmonizes with them, follow the movement accordingly, and thus generate disease. Therefore, when the upper force prevails, Heavenly Qi descends; when the lower force prevails, Earthly Qi ascends. The degree of difference depends on their quantity: slight differences cause minor imbalances, while great differences lead to major ones. In severe cases, positions and qi exchange places, resulting in significant changes and the onset of disease. The Essentials say: A severe imbalance is recorded as five divisions, a minor one as seven; the difference becomes evident—this is what it means.

154
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	六元正紀... :	
帝曰：善。論言熱無犯熱，寒無犯寒，余欲不遠寒，不遠熱，柰何。歧伯曰：悉乎哉問也。發表不遠熱，攻裏不遠寒。帝曰：不發不攻而犯寒犯熱，何如。歧伯曰：寒熱內賊，其病益甚。帝曰：願聞無病者何如。歧伯曰：無者生之，有者甚之。帝曰：生者何如。歧伯曰：不遠熱則熱至，不遠寒則寒至。寒至，則堅否腹滿，痛急下利之病生矣。熱至，則身熱吐下霍亂，癰疽瘡瘍，瞀鬱注下，瞤瘛腫脹，嘔，鼽衂頭痛，骨節變，肉痛血溢血泄，淋閟之病生矣。帝曰：治之柰何。歧伯曰：時必順之，犯者治以勝也。黃帝問曰：婦人重身，毒之何如。歧伯曰：有故無殞，亦無殞也。帝曰：願聞其故何謂也。歧伯曰：大積大聚，其可犯也，衰其太半而止，過者死。帝曰：善。鬱之甚者治之柰何。歧伯曰：木鬱達之，火鬱發之，土鬱奪之，金鬱泄之，水鬱折之，然調其氣，過者折之，以其畏也，所謂寫之。帝曰：假者何如。歧伯曰：有假其氣，則無禁也。所謂主氣不足客氣勝也。帝曰：至哉聖人之道，天地大化運行之節，臨御之紀，陰陽之政，寒暑之今，非夫子孰能通之，請藏之靈蘭之室，署曰六元正紀，非齋戒不敢示，慎傳也。
		The Emperor said: Well said. The discussion says, "When heat prevails, avoid further heat; when cold prevails, avoid more cold." I wish to neither distance myself from cold nor from heat. How should I do this? Qibo said: What a thorough question! For exterior release, do not avoid heat; for interior attack, do not avoid cold. The Emperor asked: If one neither releases the exterior nor attacks the interior but still encounters cold or heat, what then? Qibo said: Cold and heat invading internally as thieves will make the disease even worse. The Emperor asked: I wish to hear how it is for those without illness. Qibo said: Where there is none, disease may arise; where there already is, the condition will worsen. The Emperor asked: What does it mean for disease to "arise"? Qibo said: If one does not avoid heat, then heat will come; if one does not avoid cold, then cold will come. When cold arrives, symptoms such as hardness and obstruction in the abdomen, fullness, pain, urgency, and diarrhea arise. When heat arrives, symptoms such as body fever, vomiting and diarrhea with dysentery, abscesses and sores, mental confusion and obstruction of downward flow, twitching convulsions and swelling, vomiting, nasal discharge or nosebleeds, headache, bone-joint changes, muscle pain, blood overflow and bleeding from the bowels, and urinary retention arise. The Emperor asked: How should these be treated? Qibo said: Treatment must follow the season; for violations, treat with what overcomes them. The Yellow Emperor asked: For pregnant women, how should toxic substances be used? Qibo said: If there is a medical need, the fetus will not necessarily be lost; if there is no need, it also will not be lost. The Emperor asked: I wish to hear what you mean by "medical need." Qibo said: In cases of severe accumulation or aggregation, it is permissible to use strong treatments. Reduce the condition by about half and then stop; exceeding this will lead to death. The Emperor said: Well said. What should be done for severe cases of stagnation? Qibo said: For wood stagnation, promote its dispersion; for fire stagnation, release it outward. For earth stagnation, remove or reduce it; for metal stagnation, drain it off; for water stagnation, break up the obstruction. Thus regulate the qi. If excessive, break it according to what it fears—this is called purging. The Emperor asked: What about false or deceptive conditions? Qibo said: If the qi appears falsely, there are no restrictions. This refers to when the dominant seasonal Qi is insufficient and the visiting (external) Qi prevails. The Emperor said: How profound is the way of the sage! The great transformations and movements of Heaven and Earth, the regulations for governing them, the governance of Yin and Yang, the commands of cold and heat—without you, who could understand these? Please preserve this in the Linglan Hall. Title it "Liu Yuan Zheng Ji" (The Six Fundamental Principles). Without fasting and purification, I dare not reveal it; this is to be transmitted with caution. 

72. 刺法論 Treatise on Acupuncture Methods

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺法論:	
黃帝問曰：升降不前，氣交有變，即成暴鬱，余已知之。如何預救生靈，可得卻乎？歧伯稽首再拜對曰：昭乎哉問！臣聞夫子言，既明天元，須窮法刺，可以折鬱扶運，補弱全真，盛蠲餘，令除斯苦。帝曰：願卒聞之。歧伯曰：升之不前，即有甚凶也。木欲升而天柱窒抑之，木欲發鬱亦須待時，當刺足厥陰之井。火欲升而天蓬窒抑之，火欲發鬱亦須待時，君火相火同刺包絡之滎。土欲升而天衝窒抑之，土欲發鬱亦須待時，當刺足太陰之俞。金欲升而天英窒抑之，金欲發鬱亦須時，當刺手太陰之經。水欲升而天內窒抑之，水欲發鬱亦須待時，當刺足少陰之合。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "When ascending and descending do not proceed normally, when the intermingling of qi undergoes change, it immediately becomes a sudden stagnation. I already know this." How can one foresee and rescue living beings from such conditions? Is it possible to ward them off? Qibo bowed respectfully twice in succession and replied: "What a clear question!" I have heard my master say that once one understands the heavenly yuan, it is necessary to thoroughly investigate the methods of acupuncture. These can be used to dispel stagnation and support the movement of qi, strengthen the weak, preserve the true essence, eliminate excesses, and thus relieve this suffering. The emperor said: "I wish to hear it in full." Qibo said: "When ascending does not proceed, there will be a very serious calamity." If wood wishes to ascend but is obstructed by the heavenly pillar, and if wood wishes to release stagnation it must also wait for the right time; at such times one should needle the Jing point of the Foot-Jueyin meridian. If fire wishes to ascend but is obstructed and suppressed by Tianpeng, and if fire wishes to release stagnation it also must wait for the right time. Both the sovereign fire and ministerial fire should be treated by needling the Ying point of the Pericardium meridian. If earth wishes to ascend but is obstructed or suppressed by Tianchong, and if earth wishes to release stagnation it too must wait for the right time; one should needle the Shu point of the Foot-Taiyin meridian. If metal wishes to ascend but is obstructed, or suppressed by Tianying, and if metal wishes to release stagnation, it also requires the right timing; one should needle the Jing point of Hand-Taiyin meridian at that time. If water wishes to ascend but is obstructed from within by Tiannei, and if water wishes to release stagnation it still must wait for the right moment; one should needle the He point of the Foot-Shaoyin meridian.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺法論:	
帝曰：升之不前，可以預備，願聞其降，可以先防。歧伯曰：既明其升，必達其降也。升降之道，皆可先治也。木欲降而地皛窒抑之，降而不入，抑之鬱發，散而可得位，降而鬱發，暴如天閒之待時也，降而不下，鬱可速矣，降可折其所勝也，當刺手太陰之所出，刺手陽明之所入。火欲降而地玄窒抑之，降而不入，抑之鬱發，散而可矣，當折其所勝，可散其鬱，當刺足少陰之所出，刺足太陽之所入。土欲降而地蒼窒抑之，降而不下，抑之鬱發，散而可入，當折其勝，可散其鬱，當刺足厥陰之所出，刺足少陽之所入。金欲降而地彤窒抑之，降而不下，散抑之鬱發，散而可入，當折其勝，可散其鬱，當刺心包胳所出，刺手少陽所入也。水欲降而地阜窒抑之，降而不下，抑之鬱發，散而可入，當折其土，可散其鬱，當刺足太陰之所出，刺足陽明之所入。
		The emperor said: "If ascending does not proceed normally, it can be anticipated and prepared for in advance. I would now like to hear about descending—can we also prepare beforehand to prevent its problems?" Qibo said: "Having understood the principles of ascending, one must necessarily understand those of descending as well." The principles governing both ascent and descent can all be addressed in advance through treatment. If wood wishes to descend but is obstructed and restrained by Dikao, if it descends yet does not enter properly, the suppression will lead to stagnation. When dispersed, it may find its proper position; when descending with stagnation, it erupts suddenly like a condition waiting for the right moment in the heavens. If wood fails to descend at all, stagnation can occur quickly. To counter this, one should treat what is excessive by needling the Shuchu point of Hand-Taiyin and the Shuru point of Hand-Yangming. If fire wishes to descend but is obstructed or restrained by Dixuan, and it descends yet does not properly enter, the suppression will cause stagnation. Once dispersed, this condition can be resolved; one should counter what is excessive in order to disperse the stagnation. At such times, one should needle the Shuchu point of Foot-Shaoyin and the Shuru point of Foot-Taiyang. If earth wishes to descend but is obstructed by Dicang, and it descends yet fails to proceed downward, the suppression will result in stagnation. Once dispersed, it can properly enter; one should counter its excesses to disperse this stagnation. At such a time, one should needle the Shuchū point of Foot-Jueyin and the Shurù point of Foot-Shaoyang. If metal wishes to descend but is obstructed from below by Ditung, and it descends yet still does not proceed downward, the stagnation caused by suppression will be dispersed and can then properly enter. One should counter its excess in order to release this stagnation; at such a time one should needle the Shuchù point of Heart-Pericardium and the Shurù point of Hand-Shaoyang. If water wishes to descend but is obstructed below by Difu, and it descends yet remains unable to proceed downward, the stagnation resulting from suppression will be dispersed and may then properly enter. One should address its excess in order to relieve this stagnation; at such times one should needling the Shuchū point of the Foot-Taiyyn meridian and the Shurù point o fthe Foot-Yangming meridian.

3
打開字典
	刺法論:	
帝曰：五運之至，有前後與升降往來，有所承抑之，可得聞乎？刺法歧伯曰：當取其化源也。是故太過取之，不及資之。太過取之，次抑其鬱，取其運之化源，令折鬱氣。不及扶資，以扶運氣，以避虛邪也。資取之法令出《密語》。
		The emperor asked: "Regarding the arrival of the Five Periodic Energies, there are variations in their timing as well as movements of ascent and descent. When one is restrained by another, may I hear about this?" Qibo said: "One should treat the source of transformation." Therefore, when there is excess one must take from it, and when there is deficiency one must support it. When there is excessive energy, one should reduce it; secondly, suppress its stagnation by addressing the source of transformation for that movement, thus dispersing the stagnant qi. When there is insufficiency, one should support and nourish it in order to strengthen the movement of qi and avoid harmful external influences. The method for supporting or reducing energy is recorded in Mi Yu.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺法論:	
黃帝問曰：升降之刺，以知要，願聞司天未得遷正，使司化之失其常政，即萬化之或其皆妄。然與民為病，可得先除，欲濟群生，願聞其說。歧伯稽首再拜曰：悉乎哉問！言其至理，聖念慈憫，欲濟群生，臣乃盡陳斯道，可申洞微。太陽復布，即厥陰不遷正，不遷正氣塞於上，當寫足厥陰之所流。厥陰復布，少陰不遷正，不遷正即氣塞於上，當刺心包胳脈之所流。少陰復布，太陰不遷正，不遷正即氣留於上，當刺足太陰之所流。太陰復布，少陽不遷正，不遷正則氣塞未通，當刺手少陽之所流。少陽復布，則陽明不遷正，不遷正則氣未通上，當刺手太陰之所流。陽明復布，太陽不遷正，不遷正則復塞其氣，當刺足少陰之所流。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "Regarding acupuncture for ascent and descent, I now understand the essentials. I would like to hear about situations where the Shitian has not yet properly assumed its position, causing the governing transformation energies to lose their normal functions—this leads to all transformations becoming disorderly or chaotic." "Such conditions then cause illness among the people. Can they be eliminated in advance? To benefit all living beings, I wish to hear your explanation." Qibo bowed respectfully twice and said: "What a thorough question!" You speak of the ultimate principles, with your holy mind full of compassion and concern for all living beings. To benefit them, I shall fully present this doctrine so as to clarify its profound subtleties. When Taiyang spreads again, it causes Jueyin to fail in assuming its proper position. When the proper position is not assumed, qi becomes blocked above; one should then drain the Liu point of the Foot-Jueyn meridian. When Jueyin spreads again, it prevents Shaoyin from assuming its proper position. If the proper position is not taken, qi will be blocked above; at such times one should needle and drain the Liu point of Heart-Pericadium meridian. When Shaoyin spreads again, Taiyin fails to assume its proper position; if the proper position is not attained, qi remains stagnant above. At such times one should needle and release the Liu point of Foot-Taiyin. When Taiyin spreads again, Shaoyang fails to assume its proper position. If it does not take the correct position, qi becomes obstructed and remains unsmooth; at such times one should drain the Liu point of Hand-Shaoyan. When Shaoyang spreads again, Yangming fails to assume its proper position, and if it does not take the proper position, qi remains unsmooth above. At such times one should drain the Liu points of Hand-Taiyin. When Yangming spreads again, Taiyang fails to assume its proper position and if it does not do so, the qi becomes blocked once more. At such times one should release the Liu point of Foot-Shaoyyn.

顯示相似段落
	刺法論:	
帝曰：遷正不前，以通其要，願聞不退，欲折其餘，無令過失，可得明乎？歧伯曰：氣過有餘，復作布正，是名不過位也。使地氣不得後化，新司天未可遷正，故復布化令如故也。巳亥之歲天數有餘，故厥陰不退位也，風行於上，木化布天，當刺足厥陰之所入。子午之歲，天數有餘，故少陰不退位也，熱行於上，火餘化布天，當刺手厥陰之所入。丑未之歲，天數有餘，故太陰不退位也，溼行於上，雨化布天，當刺足太陰之所入。寅申之歲，天數有餘，故少陽不退位也，熱行於上，火化布天，當刺手少陽之所入。卯酉之歲，天數有餘，故陽明不退位也，金行於上，燥化布天，當刺手太陰之所入。辰戌之歲，天數有餘，故太陽不退位也，寒行於上凜水化布天，當刺足少陰之所入。故天地氣逆，化成民病，以法刺之，預可平痾。
		The emperor asked: "When the proper position is not achieved, one must clear its essentials. I now wish to hear about cases where it does not retreat—how can we dispel any remaining excesses without causing further harm? Can you make this clear?" Qibo said: "When the qi has excessive surplus and returns to spread again, it is called 'not having passed its position.'" This causes the earth qi not to proceed with its subsequent transformation, and the new Shitian cannot yet assume its proper position. Therefore, it spreads again as before, continuing its former influence. In the years of Ji and Hai, the heavenly numbers are in excess; therefore, Jueyin does not vacate its position. Wind prevails above, and wood transformation spreads through heaven; at such times one should needle into the Shuru point of the Foot-Jueyi meridian. In the years of Zi and Wu, the heavenly numbers are excessive; therefore, Shaoyin does not vacate its post. Heat prevails above, fire transformation spreads through heaven, at such times one should needle to the Shuru point of Hand-Jueyin. In the years of Chou and Wei, the heavenly numbers are in surplus; therefore, Taiyin does not vacate its place. Dampness prevails above, rain transformation spreads through heaven. At such times one should needle into Shuru point of Foot-Taiyn. In the years of Yin and Shen, the heavenly numbers are in abundance; therefore, Shaoyang does not vacate its position. Heat prevails above and fire transformation spreads through heaven. At this time one should needle into the Shurù point of the Hand-Shaoyáng meridian. In the years of Mao and You, the heavenly numbers are in surplu; therefore, Yangming does not vacate its position. Metal prevails above, dryness transformation spreads through heaven. At that time one should needle into Shurù point of Hand-Taiyn meridian. In the years of Chen and Xu, the heavenly numbers are in exces; therefore, Taiyang does not vacate its position. Cold prevails above, severe water transformation spreads through heaven. At suh times one should needle into Shru point of Foot-Shaoyn meridian. Therefore, when the qi of heaven and earth is reversed, transformation leads to illness among the people; by applying acupuncture according to these methods, one can anticipate and eliminate disorders in advance.

	刺法論:	
黃帝問曰：剛柔二干，失守其位，使天運之氣皆虛乎？與民為病，可得平乎？歧伯曰：深乎哉問！明其奧旨，天地迭移，三年化疫，是謂根之可見，必有逃門。
		The Yellow Emperor asked: "When the two heavenly stems of rigidity and softness fail to maintain their proper positions, does this cause all the energies of the celestial movement to become deficient?" Will it then lead to illness among the people? Can such conditions be balanced or resolved?" Qibo said: "What a profound question!" You have understood its profound meaning. When heaven and earth alternate in their movements, an epidemic will arise over three years; this is called the root becoming evident, and there must necessarily be a way to escape or resolve it.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺法論:	
假令甲子，剛柔失守，剛未正，柔孤而有虧，時序不令，即音律非從，如此三年，變大疫也。詳其微甚，察其淺深，欲至而可刺，刺之，當先補腎俞，次三日，可刺足太陰之所注。又有下位己卯不至，而甲子孤立者，次三年作土癘，其法補瀉，一如甲子同法也。其刺以畢，又不須夜行及遠行，令七日潔，清淨齋戒。所有自來腎有久病者，可以寅時面向南，淨神不亂，思閉氣不息七徧，以引頸嚥氣順之，如嚥氣甚硬物，如此七徧後，餌舌下津令無數。
		For example, in the year Jiazi, if the rigid and soft heavenly stems fail to maintain their positions—when rigidity has not properly established itself, and softness is isolated with a deficiency—the sequence of seasons becomes abnormal, and the pitch standards do not follow correctly. In such cases, after three years, there will be a major epidemic. Carefully examine the subtle severity and assess its depth. When it is about to arrive, acupuncture can be applied. First, one should tonify the Shu points of the Kidney; three days later, needle the Zhu point of the Foot-Taiyn meridian. There is also a case where the lower position Ji Mao does not arrive, and Jiazi remains isolated. In this situation, after three years an earth-related epidemic will arise; the method of tonification and drainage should be exactly the same as for Jiazi. After completing the acupuncture treatment, one must not travel at night or go far away; for seven days, maintain cleanliness and observe a period of purification with abstinence. For those who have long-standing kidney illness, at the hour of Yin one should face south, calm the spirit and remain undisturbed. Focus on holding one's breath without exhaling for seven repetitions, then stretch the neck while swallowing qi in a smooth manner, as if swallowing something solid. After doing this seven times, allow saliva to accumulate under the tongue and swallow it repeatedly without limit.

	刺法論:	
假令丙寅，剛柔失守，上剛干失守，下柔不可獨主之，中水運非太過，不可執法而定之，布天有餘，而失守上正，天地不合，即律呂音異，如此即天運失序，後三年變疫。詳其微甚，差有大小，徐至即後三年，至甚即首三年，當先補心俞，次五日，可刺腎之所入。又有下位地甲子，辛巳柔不附剛，亦名失守，即地運皆虛，後三年變水癘，即刺法皆如此矣。其刺如畢，慎其大喜欲情於中，如不忌，即其氣復散也，令靜七日，心欲實，令少思。
		For example, in the year Bingyin, if the rigid and soft stems fail to maintain their proper positions—when the upper rigid stem is not properly held, and the lower soft cannot independently assume leadership—the central water movement is not excessive; one must therefore not rigidly apply fixed rules. The heavenly energy appears abundant but fails to hold its correct position above, leading to a mismatch between heaven and earth, causing pitch standards to become abnormal. In such cases, the celestial order becomes disordered, and after three years an epidemic will arise. Carefully examine the subtlety and severity of the condition; there will be differences in magnitude. If it arrives gradually, the epidemic will occur after three years; if severe, it may appear as early as the first year. In such cases one should first tonify the Shu points o fthe Heart, then five days later needle into the Shru point of Kidney meridian. There is also the case where, in the lower position of Dijiazi, Xinsi's softness does not adhere to rigidity; this too is called a failure to maintain its proper position. In such cases all earth movements become deficient, and after three years there will be an epidemic related to water. The acupuncture method for treating it follows exactly the same principles. After completing this acupuncture treatment, one must be cautious about excessive joy or emotional desires within. If such emotions are not avoided, the qi will once again disperse. For seven days, remain calm and still; cultivate a solid mind and minimize thoughts.

	刺法論:	
假令庚辰，剛柔失守，上位失守，下位無合，乙庚金運，故非相招，布天未退，中運勝來，上下相錯，謂之失守，姑洗林鍾，商音不應也，如此則天運化易，三年變大疫。詳其天數，差有微甚，微即微，三年至，甚即甚，三年至，當先肝俞，次三日，可刺肺之所行。刺畢，可靜神七日，慎勿大怒，怒必真氣卻散之。又或在下地甲子乙未失守者，即乙柔干，即上庚獨治之，亦名失守者，即□□孤主之，三年變癘，名曰金癘，其至待時也，詳其地數之等差，亦推其微甚，可知遲速爾。諸位乙庚失守，刺法同，肝欲平，即勿怒。
		For example, in the year Gengchen, if the rigid and soft elements fail to maintain their positions—the upper position is lost, and there is no harmony with the lower one—since Yigeng belongs to metal movement, they are not properly connected. The heavenly influence has yet to recede, while the central movement prevails, causing a mismatch between above and below; this is called "failure to hold its position." In such cases, Gushan and Linzhong tones do not correspond with the Shang pitch. When this occurs, the transformation of celestial energy becomes disordered, leading to a major epidemic after three years. Carefully examine the heavenly numbers; there are subtle differences in severity. If it is mild, the condition will arrive after three years; if serious, it will also manifest within three years. In such cases one should initially tonify the Shu point of Liver, then on the third day needle the Xing points of Lung meridian. After completing the acupuncture, one should remain calm and tranquil for seven days; be careful not to become greatly angry, as anger will certainly cause true qi to disperse. There is also the case in which, at the lower level of Dijiazi and Yiwèi, there is a failure to maintain position. Here, the soft stem Yi fails to hold its place, while Geng above governs alone; this too is called "failure to maintain one's post." In such situations, an isolated force assumes leadership, leading to an epidemic after three years, known as a metal-related plague. The arrival of this condition awaits the right time. Carefully examine the differences in earthly numbers and assess their subtlety or severity; from this, you can determine whether it will arrive quickly or slowly. For all cases where Yi and Geng fail to maintain their posts, the acupuncture method is the same. To balance the Liver, one must avoid anger.

	刺法論:	
假令壬午，剛柔失守，上壬未遷正，下丁獨然，即雖陽年，虧及不同，上下失守，相招其有期，差之微甚，各有其數也，律呂二角，失而不和，同音有日，微甚如見，三年大疫，當刺脾之俞，次三日，可刺肝之所出也。刺畢，靜神七日，勿大醉歌樂，其氣復散，又勿飽食，勿食生物，欲令脾實，氣無滯飽，無久坐，食無太酸，無食一切生物，宜甘宜淡。又或地下甲子，丁酉失守其位，未得中司，即氣不當位，下不與壬奉合者，亦名失守，非名合德，故柔不附剛，即地運不合，三年變癘，其刺法一如木疫之法。

The two Jiao tones of Lülü become discordant and out of harmony. Although similar pitches exist on specific days, when such discrepancies appear clearly, a major epidemic will arise after three years. There is also the case of Dijiazi at the lower level, where Dingyou fails to maintain its position and does not properly assume the central governing role. In this situation, qi does not occupy its proper place; the lower cannot harmonize with Ren above. This too is called "failure to hold one's post," as it lacks harmony in virtue. Therefore, softness does not adhere to firmness, leading to disharmony in earthly movement and an epidemic after three years. The acupuncture method should follow exactly that used for a wood-related plague.

假令戊申，剛柔失守，戊癸雖火運，陽年不太過也，上失其剛，柔地獨主，其氣不正，故有邪干，迭移其位，差有淡深，欲至將合，音律先同，如此天運失時，三年之中，火疫至矣，當刺肺之俞。刺畢，靜神七日，勿大悲傷也，悲傷即肺動，而真氣復散也，人欲實肺者，要在息氣也。又或地下甲子，癸亥失守者，即柔失守位也，即上失其剛也，即亦名戊癸不相合德者也，即運與地虛，後三年變癘，即名火癘。

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	刺法論:	
黃帝曰：余聞五疫之至，皆相染易，無問大小，病狀相似，不施救療，如何可得不相移易者？歧伯曰：不相染者，正氣存內，邪不可干，避其毒氣，天牝從來，復得其往，氣出於腦，即不邪干。氣出於腦，即室先想心如日。欲將入於疫室，先想青氣自肝而出，左行於東，化作林木。次想白氣自肺而出，右行於西，化作戈甲。次想赤氣自心而出，南行於上，化作燄明。次想黑氣自腎而出，北行於下，化作水。次想黃氣自脾而出，存於中央，化作土。五氣護身之畢，以想頭上如北斗之煌煌，然後可入於疫室。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "I have heard that when the five epidemics arrive, they all easily transmit from one to another. Regardless of age or size, their symptoms are similar. If no treatment is applied, how can it be possible for them not to spread and affect others?" Qibo said: "Those who do not become infected are those in whom the true qi resides within, making it impossible for external evils to invade. Avoiding toxic influences and following the natural path of Tianpin from its origin to its destination, with qi emerging from the brain—this is how one remains free from evil invasion." When qi emerges from the brain, first visualize the heart as a sun. If one is about to enter an epidemic-infected room, first imagine green qi emerging from the Liver and flowing leftward toward the east, transforming into a forest of trees. Next, visualize white qi arising from the Lungs, moving rightward toward the west, and transforming into weapons and armor. Then imagine red qi emerging from the Heart, flowing upward to the south, and transforming into bright flames. Next, visualize black qi arising from the Kidneys, moving downward toward the north, and transforming into water. Then imagine yellow qi emerging from the Spleen, residing in the center, and transforming into earth. Once these five qi energies have fully protected the body, visualize above your head a brilliance like that of the Big Dipper; only then may one enter an epidemic-infected room.
