打開字典
顯示相似段落
	至真要大... :	
帝曰：六氣之復何如。歧伯曰：悉乎哉問也。厥陰之復，少腹堅滿，裏急暴痛，偃木飛沙，倮蟲不榮，厥心痛，汗發嘔吐，飲食不入，入而復出，筋骨掉眩，清厥，甚則入脾，食痺而吐。衝陽絕，死不治。少陰之復，燠熱內作，煩躁鼽嚏，少腹絞痛，火見燔焫嗌燥，分注時止，氣動於左，上行於右，欬，皮膚痛，暴瘖心痛鬱冒不知人，乃洒淅惡寒振慄譫妄，寒已而熱，渴而欲飲，少氣骨痿隔腸不便，外為浮腫噦噫，赤氣後化，流水不冰，熱氣大行，介蟲不復，病疿胗瘡瘍癰疽痤痔，甚則入肺，欬而鼻淵。天府絕，死不治。太陰之復，濕變乃舉，體重中滿，食飲不化，陰氣上厥，胸中不便，飲發於中，欬喘有聲，大雨時行，鱗見於陸，頭頂痛重，而掉瘛尤甚，嘔而密默唾吐清液，甚則入腎，竅寫無度。太谿絕，死不治。少陽之復，大熱將至，枯燥燔爇，介蟲乃耗，驚瘛欬衄，心熱煩躁，便數憎風，厥氣上行，面如浮埃，目乃瞤瘛，火氣內發，上為口糜嘔逆，血溢血泄，發而為瘧，惡寒鼓慄，寒極反熱，嗌絡焦槁，渴引水漿，色變黃赤，少氣脈萎，化而為水，傳為胕腫，甚則入肺，欬而血泄。尺澤絕，死不治。陽明之復，清氣大舉，森木蒼乾，毛蟲乃厲，病生胠脇，氣歸於左，善太息，甚則心痛否滿，腹脹而泄，嘔苦欬噦煩心，病在鬲中頭痛，甚則入肝，驚駭筋攣。太衝絕，死不治。太陽之復，厥氣上行，水凝雨冰，羽蟲乃死，心胃生寒，胸膈不利，心痛否滿，頭痛善悲，時眩仆，食減腰脽反痛，屈伸不便，地裂冰堅，陽光不治，少腹控睾，引腰脊上衝心，唾出清水及為噦噫，甚則入心，善忘善悲。神門絕，死不治。帝曰：善。治之柰何。歧伯曰：厥陰之復，治以酸寒，佐以甘辛，以酸寫之，以甘緩之。少陰之復，治以鹹寒，佐以苦辛，以甘寫之，以酸收之，辛苦發之，以鹹耎之。太陰之復，治以苦熱，佐以酸辛，以苦寫之，燥之泄之。少陽之復，治以鹹冷，佐以苦辛，以鹹耎之，以酸收之，辛苦發之，發不遠熱，無犯溫涼，少陰同法。陽明之復，治以辛溫，佐以苦甘，以苦泄之，以苦下之，以酸補之。太陽之復，治以鹹熱，佐以甘辛，以苦堅之。治諸勝復，寒者熱之，熱者寒之，溫者清之，清者溫之，散者收之，抑者散之，燥者潤之，急者緩之，堅者耎之，脆者堅之，衰者補之，強者寫之，各安其氣，必清必靜，則病氣衰去，歸其所宗，此治之大體也。
		The Emperor asked: "What about the recurrence of the six qi?" Qibo said: "How thorough your question is." When Jueyin recurs, the lower abdomen becomes firm and distended with internal urgency and sudden pain. Trees bend over and sand flies through the air; hairless creatures do not thrive. There is heart pain accompanied by sweating, vomiting, inability to take in food, or if taken in, it is expelled again. The tendons and bones tremble and dizziness occurs. Coldness affects the extremities. In severe cases, it enters the spleen, causing obstruction of digestion with vomiting. If the Chongyang pulse is cut off, death will occur and treatment will be ineffective. When Shaoyin recurs, internal heat arises with irritability and restlessness; there are sneezing and nasal discharge. Severe pain occurs in the lower abdomen; fire manifests as burning sensations and dryness of the throat. Urination is intermittent. Qi movement begins on the left side and ascends to the right. Coughing, skin pain, sudden aphonia (loss of voice), heart pain with mental confusion and unconsciousness occur. One experiences chills, shivering, delirium, followed by heat, thirst, and a desire for drink. There is shortness of breath, bone atrophy, intestinal obstruction, difficulty in defecation; externally there is edema, belching, and hiccups. After the red qi transforms, flowing water does not freeze, and intense heat spreads widely. Shelled creatures do not reproduce again. Diseases such as swellings, abscesses, carbuncles, furuncles, and hemorrhoids arise. In severe cases it enters the lungs, causing coughing with nasal discharge. If the Tianfu pulse is cut off, death occurs and treatment is ineffective. When Taiyin recurs, dampness transformation arises; the body feels heavy and distended in the middle with undigested food. Yin qi rises upward to cause obstruction of the chest. Fluids accumulate within, causing coughing and wheezing with audible sounds. Heavy rains occur at times. Scaled creatures appear on land. There is headache and heaviness at the vertex; dizziness and convulsions become particularly severe. Nausea occurs along with frequent spitting up of clear fluids. In serious cases it enters the kidney, causing uncontrolled discharge from orifices. If the Taixi pulse is cut off, death results and treatment is ineffective. When Shaoyang recurs, intense heat is about to arrive; dryness and fire scorch the environment. Shelled creatures become exhausted. There are startlements, convulsions, coughing, nosebleeds, internal heart-heat with irritability, frequent urination, aversion to wind, upward movement of rebellious qi, a face covered in fine dust-like appearance, eye twitching and convulsions. Fire qi arises internally, causing oral ulcers, vomiting, blood spitting, bloody diarrhea; it manifests as malaria with chills and shivering. Extreme cold turns into heat; the throat vessels become scorched and dry, leading to intense thirst for water. The complexion changes to yellow or red; there is shortness of breath and atrophy of the pulse. It transforms into fluid retention, resulting in edema. In severe cases it affects the lungs, causing cough with blood discharge. If the Chize pulse is cut off, death ensues and treatment is ineffective. When Yangming recurs, cold qi rises greatly; trees become dry and withered. Hairy creatures suffer from plagues. The disease arises in the hypochondriac area, with qi gathering on the left side. Frequent deep sighing occurs. In severe cases there is heart pain and obstruction of fullness, abdominal distension with diarrhea, vomiting of bitter substances, coughing, hiccups, irritability of the mind; the illness resides between the diaphragm causing headache. If it enters the liver in serious cases, startlement and convulsions of tendons occur. If the Taichong pulse is cut off, death follows and treatment is ineffective. When Taiyang recurs, rebellious cold qi rises upward; water congeals and rain turns to ice. Winged creatures die. Cold arises in the heart and stomach with obstruction of the chest and diaphragm, causing heart pain and fullness. Headaches occur along with frequent sadness and occasional dizziness or fainting. Appetite decreases while the waist and hips experience counter-pain; bending and stretching become difficult. The earth cracks and ice becomes hard; sunlight cannot penetrate. There is pulling sensation in the lower abdomen affecting the testicles, radiating up to the waist, spine, and chest with a feeling of upward pressure toward the heart. Clear saliva is expectorated along with belching and hiccups. In severe cases it invades the heart, causing forgetfulness and excessive sadness. If the Shenmen pulse is cut off, death occurs, and treatment is ineffective. The Emperor said: "Good." How should they be treated? Qibo said: "When Jueyin recurs excessively, treat with sour-cold substances; assist with sweet-pungent ones. Use sour to purge and sweet to relieve." When Shaoyin recurs excessively, treat it with salty-cold substances; assist with bitter- and pungent-flavored ones. Use sweet to purge, sour to astringe, pungent-bitter to disperse, and salty to soften. When Taiyin recurs excessively, treat it using bitter-warm substances; assist with sour-pungent ones. Employ bitter to purge and dryness to drain. When Shaoyang recurs excessively, treat with salty-cold substances; support with bitter and pungent ones. Use salty to soften, sour to astringe; use bitter-pungent to disperse. When dispersing, do not move far from heat; avoid offending warmth or coolness. The method for Shaoyin is the same. When Yangming recurs excessively, treat with pungent-warm substances; assist with bitter and sweet ones. Use bitter to drain and purge, and sour to tonify. When Taiyang recurs excessively, treat with salty-warm substances; support with sweet-pungent ones; use bitter to consolidate. In treating the mutual overcoming and recurrence of the six qi, treat cold with warmth, heat with cooling, warmth with clearing, coolness with warming, dispersion with astringency, suppression with dispersal, dryness with moistening, urgency with relaxation, hardness with softening, fragility with strengthening, deficiency with tonification, excess with purging. Each treatment should harmonize the qi; it must be clear and tranquil so that pathogenic influences weaken and dissipate, returning to their original source. This is the general principle of treatment.

	至真要大... :	
帝曰；善。氣之上下，何謂也。歧伯曰：身半以上，其氣三矣，天之分也，天氣主之。身半以下，其氣三矣，地之分也，地氣主之。以名命氣，以氣命處，而言其病，半所謂天樞也。故上勝而下俱病者，以地名之，下勝而上俱病者，以天名之。所謂勝至報氣，屈伏而未發也，復至則不以天地異名，皆如復氣為法也。
		The Emperor said: Good. What is meant by the upward and downward movement of qi? Qibo said: "Above the middle part of the body, there are three divisions of qi; this is the division of heaven, governed by heavenly qi." Below the middle part of the body lie three divisions of qi as well, which belong to the division of earth and are governed by earthly qi. Naming the qi according to its name, assigning locations based on the nature of the qi, and describing diseases in this way—this is what is meant by "Tianshu," or the celestial pivot. Therefore, when upper qi prevails and lower parts are all affected by illness, it is named according to the earth. When lower qi prevails and upper parts are all afflicted with disease, it is named after heaven. What is referred to as the "arrival of overcoming qi and the response qi" means that the suppressed pathogenic influence has not yet manifested. When it recurs, it does not differ in name according to heaven or earth; all cases follow the method for recurring qi.

顯示相似段落
	至真要大... :	
帝曰：勝復之動，時有常乎，氣有必乎。歧伯曰：時有常位，而氣無必也。帝曰：願聞其道也。歧伯曰：初氣終三氣，天氣主之，勝之常也。四氣盡終氣，地氣主之，復之常也。有勝則復，無勝則否。帝曰：善。復已而勝何如。歧伯曰：勝至則復，無常數也，衰乃止耳。復已而勝，不復則害，此傷生也。帝曰：復而反病何也。歧伯曰：居非其位，不相得也，大復其勝則主勝之，故反病也，所謂火燥熱也。帝曰：治之何如。歧伯曰：夫氣之勝也，微者隨之，甚者制之，氣之復也，和者平之，暴者奪之，皆隨勝氣，安其屈伏，無問其數，以平為期，此其道也。
		The Emperor asked: "In the movement of overcoming and recurrence, is there a fixed time? Is there an inevitable pattern to the qi?" Qibo said: "There are fixed positions for times, but the qi does not follow any absolute rule." The Emperor said: "I wish to hear about its principles." Qibo said: "From the first qi to the third qi, heavenly qi governs; this is the usual pattern of overcoming." The fourth through seventh qis are governed by earthly qi; this is the regular pattern for recurrence. If there is overcoming, then there will be recurrence; if there is no overcoming, there will not be recurrence. The Emperor said: "Good." What happens when overcoming occurs after recurrence? Qibo said: "When overcoming arises, recurrence follows; there is no fixed number of times. It stops only when the qi declines." If after recurrence there is again overcoming and if it does not recur further, harm will result; this injures life. The Emperor asked: "Why do diseases arise from recurrence?" Qibo said: "When a qi resides in an inappropriate position, it becomes incompatible. When the recurrence strongly overcomes its counterpart, it dominates and causes illness; thus disease arises. This is what is referred to as 'fire dryness-heat.'" The Emperor asked: "How should this be treated?" Qibo said: "When a qi prevails, if it is mild, follow and adapt to it; if severe, control it. When recurrence occurs, if the condition is harmonious, regulate it toward balance; if violent, counteract it forcefully. In all cases, treatment should follow the prevailing qi, stabilize its suppressed state, without concern for how many times it recurs, aiming only at achieving equilibrium. This is the principle."

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	至真要大... :	
帝曰：善。客主之勝復柰何。歧伯曰：客主之氣，勝而無復也。帝曰：其逆從何如。歧伯曰：主勝逆，客勝從，天之道也。帝曰：其生病何如。歧伯曰：厥陰司天，客勝則耳鳴掉眩，甚則欬，主勝則胸脇痛，舌難以言。少陰司天，客勝則鼽嚏頸項強，肩背瞀熱，頭痛少氣發熱耳聾目瞑，甚則胕腫血溢，瘡瘍欬喘，主勝則心熱煩躁，甚則脇痛支滿。太陰司天，客勝則首面胕腫，呼吸氣喘，主勝則胸腹滿，食已而瞀。少陽司天，客勝則丹胗外發，及為丹熛瘡瘍，嘔逆喉痺，頭痛嗌腫，耳聾血溢，內為瘛瘲，主勝則胸滿欬仰息，甚而有血，手熱。陽明司天，清復內餘，則欬衂嗌塞，心鬲中熱，欬不止而白血出者死。太陽司天，客勝則胸中不利，出清涕感寒則欬，主勝則喉嗌中鳴。厥陰在泉，客勝則大關節不利，內為痙強拘瘛，外為不便，主勝則筋骨繇併，腰腹時痛。少陰在泉，客勝則腰痛尻股膝髀腨䯒足病，瞀熱以酸，胕腫不能久立，溲便變，主勝則厥氣上行，心痛發熱，鬲中眾痺皆作，發於胠脇魄汗不藏，四逆而起。太陰在泉，客勝則足痿下重，便溲不時，濕客下焦，發而濡寫，及為腫隱曲之疾，主勝則寒氣逆滿，食飲不下，甚則為疝。少陽在泉，客勝則腰腹痛而反惡寒，甚則下白溺白，主勝則熱反上行而客於心，心痛發熱，格中而嘔，少陰同候。陽明在泉，客勝則清氣動下，少腹堅滿而數便寫，主勝則腰重腹痛，少腹生寒下為鶩溏，則寒厥於腸，上衝胸中，甚則喘不能久立。太陽在泉，寒復內餘，則腰尻痛屈伸不利，股脛足膝中痛。帝曰：善。治之柰何。歧伯曰：高者抑之，下者舉之，有餘折之，不足補之，佐以所利，和以所宜，必安其主客，適其寒溫，同者逆之，異者從之。帝曰：治寒以熱，治熱以寒，氣相得者逆之，不相得者從之，余以知之矣。其於正味何如。歧伯曰：木位之主，其寫以酸，其補以辛。火位之主，其寫以甘，其補以鹹。土位之主，其寫以苦，其補以甘。金位之主，其寫以辛，其補以酸。水位之主，其寫以鹹，其補以苦。厥陰之客，以辛補之，以酸寫之，以甘緩之。少陰之客，以鹹補之，以甘寫之，以鹹收之。太陰之客，以甘補之，以苦寫之，以甘緩之。少陽之客，以鹹補之，以甘寫之，以鹹耎之。陽明之客，以酸補之，以辛寫之，以苦泄之。太陽之客，以苦補之，以鹹寫之，以苦堅之，以辛潤之，開發腠理，致津液通氣也。
		The Emperor said: "Good." What about overcoming and recurrence between the guest qi and host qi? Qibo said: "The relationship between guest and host qi involves overcoming, but not necessarily recurrence." The Emperor asked: "What about their patterns of disharmony or harmony?" Qibo said: "When the host qi overcomes, it is considered inverse; when the guest qi overcomes, it follows naturally. This is in accordance with the principle of heaven." The Emperor asked: "What about the diseases they cause?" Qibo said: "When Jueyin governs the heavens, if guest qi prevails, it causes ringing in the ears, dizziness, and vertigo; severe cases may involve coughing. If host qi prevails, there is chest and hypochondriac pain with difficulty speaking." When Shaoyin governs the heavens, when guest qi prevails it causes nasal discharge and sneezing, stiffness in the neck and shoulders, dizziness with heat in the back, headache, shortness of breath, fever, deafness, and eye closure; severe cases may result in edema, blood overflow, abscesses, coughing, and wheezing. When host qi prevails it causes internal heart-heat and irritability; severe cases involve hypochondriac pain and distention of the limbs. When Taiyin governs the heavens, guest qi prevailing causes swelling of the head and face with shortness of breath and wheezing; host qi prevailing results in chest and abdominal distention, followed by dizziness after eating. When Shaoyang governs the heavens, if the guest qi prevails, red swellings appear externally on the skin and may develop into boils or sores; vomiting, throat obstruction, headache, swelling of the pharynx, deafness, blood overflow occur. Internally, it causes convulsions and spasms. If the host qi prevails, chest fullness with coughing and labored breathing occurs; in severe cases there is bloody sputum and heat in the hands. When Yangming governs the heavens, internal residual cold recurs, causing coughing, nosebleeds and throat obstruction. Heat accumulates in the heart and diaphragm area; if coughing persists with white blood discharge, death will occur. When Taiyang governs the heavens, if external (guest) qi prevails, there will be discomfort in the chest and clear nasal discharge; cold exposure causes coughing. If internal (host) qi prevails, a gurgling sound occurs in the throat and pharynx. When Jueyin is in the lower region, if external (guest) pathogenic qi prevails, large joints become stiff and movement difficult; internally it causes spasm, rigidity, and convulsions; externally there are physical impairments. If internal (host) healthy qi prevails, tendons and bones become unsteady with occasional pain in the waist and abdomen. When Shaoyin is in the lower region and external (guest) pathogenic forces prevail, there will be pain in the waist, hips, thighs, knees, calves, shins, and feet; dizziness with heat and sour taste occurs. Edema develops, making it difficult to stand for long periods, and urination and defecation patterns change. When internal (host) healthy forces prevail, rebellious qi rises upward causing heart pain and fever, numbness in the diaphragm region spreads widely, manifesting as multiple symptoms of obstruction; this arises from the hypochondriac areas with excessive sweating that cannot be controlled, leading to cold limbs spreading upward. When Taiyin is in the lower position, if external (guest) forces prevail, it causes foot atrophy and heaviness in the lower body; urination and defecation become irregular. Dampness lodges in the lower jiao region, leading to profuse diarrhea or watery stools, as well as swelling and discomfort in the genital area. If internal (host) forces prevail, cold qi moves upward causing fullness and obstruction, making it difficult for food and drink to descend; severe cases may develop into hernia. When Shaoyang is in the lower position and external (guest) forces prevail, there will be waist and abdominal pain with aversion to cold; severe cases may present with white discharge from urination. When internal (host) forces are dominant, heat rises upward into the heart region causing chest pain, fever, and vomiting due to obstruction at the cardiac orifice—this pattern is similar for Shaoyin. When Yangming is in the lower position and guest qi prevails, clear (cold) energy descends downward, causing firmness and distension of the lower abdomen with frequent loose stools. When host qi dominates, there will be heaviness in the waist, abdominal pain, cold sensation in the lower abdomen resulting in watery diarrhea; this leads to cold 厥 affecting the intestines, rising upward to press against the chest, and severe cases may cause shortness of breath making it impossible to stand for long. When Taiyang is in the lower position, internal residual cold recurs causing pain and stiffness in the waist and hips with restricted movement; there will also be pain in the thighs, shins, feet, and knees. The Emperor said: "Good." How should these conditions be treated? Qibo said: "For excess above, subdue it; for deficiency below, raise it. For excess conditions, reduce them; for deficiencies, tonify them. Support with what is beneficial and harmonize according to appropriate methods. It is essential to stabilize the relationship between host and guest qi, adjust cold and warmth appropriately, oppose similar patterns of disharmony, and follow those that are different." The Emperor said: "Treating cold with warmth and heat with cooling—when the qi is compatible, oppose it; when incompatible, follow its nature. I already understand this." What about their relationship to the five flavors? Qibo said: "For the Wood element, purging is achieved with sour flavor; tonification is done with pungent." For the Fire element, purging uses sweet flavor; tonification employs salty. For the Earth element, purging is done with bitter flavor; tonification uses sweet. For the Metal element, purging employs pungent flavor; tonification utilizes sour. For the Water element, purging is accomplished with salty flavor; tonification relies on bitter. For the guest qi of Jueyin, pungent flavors are used for tonification, sour for purging, and sweet to relieve tension. For the guest qi of Shaoyin, salty flavors are used for tonification; sweet for purging; and salty to consolidate. For the guest qi of Taiyin, sweet flavors are used for tonification; bitter for purging; and sweet to relieve tension. For the guest qi of Shaoyang, salty flavors are used for supplementation; sweet for purging; and again salty to soften. For the guest qi of Yangming, sour is used for supplementation; pungent for purging; and bitter to eliminate. For the guest qi of Taiyang, bitter is used for supplementation; salty for purging; again bitter to strengthen; and pungent to moisten. This opens the pores and channels, promoting the flow of body fluids and unblocking the qi.

	至真要大... :	
帝曰：善。願聞陰陽之三也何謂。歧伯曰：氣有多少，異用也。帝曰：陽明何謂也。歧伯曰：兩陽合明也。帝曰：厥陰何也。歧伯曰：兩陰交盡也。帝曰：氣有多少，病有盛衰，治有緩急，方有大小，願聞其約柰何。歧伯曰：氣有高下，病有遠近，證有中外，治有輕重，適其至所為故也。《大要》曰：君一臣二，奇之制也，君二臣四，偶之制也，君二臣三，奇之制也，君二臣六，偶之制也。故曰：近者奇之，遠者偶之，汗者不以奇，下者不以偶，補上治上，制以緩，補下治下，制以急，急則氣味厚，緩則氣味薄，適其至所，此之謂也。病所遠，而中道氣味之者食而過之，無越其制度也。是故平氣之道，近而奇偶，制小其服也。遠而奇偶，制大其服也。大則數少，小則數多。多則九之，少則二之。奇之不去則偶之，是謂重方。偶之不去，則反佐以取之，所謂寒熱溫涼反從其病也。帝曰：善。病生於本，余知之矣。生於標者，治之柰何。歧伯曰：病反其本，得標之病，治反其本，得標之方。
		The Emperor said: "Good." I would like to ask, what is meant by the three divisions of Yin and Yang? Qibo said: "Qi varies in quantity; this leads to different functions." The Emperor asked: "What is meant by Yangming?" Qibo said: "It means the union of two yang forces, together illuminating." The Emperor asked: "What is Jueyin?" Qibo said: "It means the complete convergence of two yin forces." The Emperor asked: "Qi varies in quantity, diseases differ in severity, treatments vary in urgency, and prescriptions range in size. I would like to know the general principles of how this should be handled." Qibo said: "Qi varies in elevation, diseases differ in depth and location, syndromes may be internal or external, and treatments vary in intensity—this is determined by the appropriate response to where the condition has manifested." The Da Yao says: "One sovereign herb with two minister herbs constitutes an odd-numbered formula; two sovereign herbs with four minister herbs constitute an even-numbered formula. Two sovereign herbs with three minister herbs is also an odd-numbered formula, and two sovereign herbs with six minister herbs is an even-numbered one." Therefore it is said: "For conditions close to the surface, use an odd-numbered formula; for those deeper or more distant, use an even-numbered one. For perspiration-inducing treatments, do not use odd-numbered formulas; for purgation, avoid even-numbered ones. When treating upper-level deficiency, employ a slow-acting formulation; when addressing lower-level deficiency, use a rapid-acting one. Rapid action requires strong and potent ingredients, while slow action employs mild and subtle ones. Adjust the strength of the formula according to where the condition is located—this is what it means." When a disease lies far from its origin and the medicinal qi reaches it along the way, the treatment should be administered in such a manner that it passes through without exceeding the established principles of formulation. Therefore, for conditions with balanced qi, when the disease is near and either an odd or even formula is used, the dosage should be small. When the condition is distant and requires either an odd or even formulation, a larger dose should be administered. A large dosage means fewer administrations; a small dosage requires more frequent ones. If frequent, administer up to nine times; if infrequent, as few as two times. If an odd-numbered formula fails to resolve the condition, switch to an even-numbered one—this is called a combined or double prescription. If an even-numbered formula also fails, then employ counter-assisting methods to achieve the desired effect—this is what is meant by using cold or heat, warmth or coolness in opposition to the disease pattern. The Emperor said: "Good." Diseases originate from the root—I understand this already. For diseases that arise from the branch, how should they be treated? Qibo said: "When a disease reverses its root and manifests as a branch condition, the diagnosis is based on the branch. Treatment should also reverse the approach to the root, following the therapeutic method appropriate for the branch."

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	至真要大... :	
帝曰：善。六氣之勝，何以候之。歧伯曰：乘其至也，清氣大來，燥之勝也，風木受邪，肝病生焉。熱氣大來，火之勝也，金燥受邪，肺病生焉。寒氣大來，水之勝也，火熱受邪，心病生焉。濕氣大來，土之勝也，寒水受邪，腎病生焉。風氣大來，木之勝也，土濕受邪，脾病生焉。所謂感邪而生病也。乘年之虛，則邪甚也。失時之和，亦邪甚也。遇月之空，亦邪甚也。重感於邪，則病危矣。有勝之氣，其必來復也。帝曰：其脈至何如。歧伯曰：厥陰之至其脈弦，少陰之至其脈鉤，太陰之至其脈沈，少陽之至大而浮，陽明之至短而濇，太陽之至大而長。至而和則平，至而甚則病，至而反者病，至而不至者病，未至而至者病，陰陽易者危。
		The Emperor said: "Good." How can we observe the dominance of the six qi? Qibo said: "When a particular qi arrives in excess, clear and dry qi becomes prominent—this indicates the dominance of dryness. Wind-wood is then affected by pathogenic influences, giving rise to liver disease." When intense heat qi arrives in great force, it indicates the dominance of fire. Metal-dryness is then affected by pathogenic factors, leading to lung disease. When intense cold qi arrives in great force, this indicates the dominance of water. Fire-heat is then affected by pathogenic forces, giving rise to heart disease. When intense damp qi arrives in great force, it signifies the dominance of earth. Cold-water is then affected by pathogenic influence, leading to kidney disease. When intense wind qi arrives in great force, it denotes the dominance of wood. Earth-dampness is then affected by pathogenic invasion, giving rise to spleen disease. These are what is meant by being affected by pathogenic factors and developing disease. When the body's defenses are weakened in a given year, external pathogens can take advantage and become severe. Losing harmony with seasonal rhythms also leads to serious pathogenic invasion. Encountering a month of weakness or emptiness (a period of diminished energy) also results in severe pathological influence. If one is repeatedly affected by pathogenic influences, the condition becomes critical. When a dominant qi exists, it will inevitably return in response. The Emperor asked: "What does the pulse pattern look like when this occurs?" Qibo said: "When Jueyin arrives, the pulse is taut; when Shaoyin arrives, it is hooke-like (hollow and rapid); when Taiyin arrives, it is deep; when Shaoyang arrives, it is large and floating; when Yangming arrives, it is short and 涩 (congested or rough); when Taiyang arrives, it is large and long." When the pulse arrives in harmony, it indicates balance; when it is excessive upon arrival, disease occurs. When the pulse pattern is opposite to what is expected, illness results. If the qi should arrive but does not, or if it arrives before its time, this also causes disease. A reversal of yin and yang patterns is dangerous.

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	至真要大... :	
帝曰：六氣標本，所從不同，柰何。歧伯曰：氣有從本者，有從標本者，有不從標本者也。帝曰：願卒聞之。歧伯曰：少陽太陰從本，少陰太陽從本從標，陽明厥陰不從標本從乎中也。故從本者，化生於本，從標本者，有標本之化，從中者，以中氣為化也。帝曰：脈從而病反者，其診何如。歧伯曰：脈至而從，按之不鼓，諸陽皆然。帝曰：諸陰之反，其脈何如。歧伯曰：脈至而從，按之鼓甚而盛也。是故百病之起，有生於本者，有生於摽者，有生於中氣者，有取本而得者，有取摽而得者，有取中氣而得者，有取摽本而得者，有逆取而得者，有從取而得者。逆，正順也。若順，逆也。故曰：知摽與本，用之不殆，明知逆順，正行無問。此之謂也。不知是者，不足以言診，足以亂經。故《大要》曰：粗工嘻嘻，以為可知，言熱未已，寒病復始，同氣異形，迷診亂經，此之謂也。夫標本之道，要而博，小而大，可以言一而知百病之害，言摽與本，易而勿損，察本與摽，氣可令調，明知勝復，為萬民式，天之道畢矣。
		The Emperor asked: "The six qi have different origins and manifestations—how should this be understood?" Qibo said: "Some conditions follow the root, some follow both the root and branch, and others do not follow either the root or the branch." The Emperor said: "I would like to hear a complete explanation of this." Qibo said: "Shaoyang and Taiyin follow the root; Shaoyin and Taiyang follow both the root and the branch; Yangming and Jueyin do not follow either root or branch, but rather follow the middle." Therefore, those that follow the root derive transformation from it; those following both root and branch exhibit transformations of both; those following the middle are transformed according to the middle qi. The Emperor asked: "When the pulse follows a certain pattern but the disease manifests oppositely, what does this indicate in diagnosis?" Qibo said: "If the pulse appears to follow a pattern but feels weak and lacks force upon palpation, this is true of all yang conditions." The Emperor asked: "For yin conditions that manifest oppositely, what would the pulse be like?" Qibo said: "When the pulse appears to follow the pattern but upon palpation is strongly forceful and vigorous, this indicates yin conditions manifesting in opposition." Therefore, the origin of all diseases may arise from the root, or from the branch, or from the middle qi. Some conditions are diagnosed and treated by addressing the root; others by targeting the branch; still others by focusing on the middle qi. There are also cases where treatment is achieved by combining both root and branch approaches, as well as those requiring counteracting methods (inverse treatment) or following the natural direction of the condition (concordant treatment). Counteraction means going against the apparent pattern to restore normal harmony. If one follows what appears natural but leads to disharmony, it is actually counterproductive. Therefore it is said: "Understanding the branch and root ensures treatment without danger; fully knowing what is inverse or concordant allows one to proceed correctly without hesitation." This is what it means. Those who do not understand this are not qualified to speak of diagnosis, and their actions will only confuse the classics. Therefore, The Da Yao says: "Unskilled physicians laugh carelessly and believe they understand. They speak of heat before finishing their diagnosis, yet cold disease begins anew. Though the qi is similar, its manifestations differ; this confuses diagnosis and disrupts the classics—this is what it refers to." The principle of root and branch is essential yet comprehensive, small in form but great in significance. Understanding it allows one to speak of a single pattern while knowing the harm of all diseases. To discuss branch and root clearly without error, and to examine both root and branch thoroughly enables the regulation of qi. Fully understanding dominance and response patterns establishes a model for the people, completing an understanding of the Dao of Heaven.

15
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	至真要大... :	
帝曰：勝復之變，早晏何如。歧伯曰：夫所勝者，勝至已病，病已慍慍，而復已萌也。夫所復者，勝盡而起，得位而甚，勝有微甚，復有少多，勝和而和，勝虛而虛，天之常也。帝曰：勝復之作，動不當位，或後時而至，其故何也。歧伯曰：夫氣之生，與其化衰盛異也。寒暑溫涼盛衰之用，其在四維。故陽之動，始於溫，盛於暑，陰之動，始於清，盛於寒。春夏秋冬，各差其分。故《大要》曰：彼春之暖，為夏之暑，彼秋之忿，為冬之怒，謹按四維，斥候皆歸，其終可見，其始可知。此之謂也。帝曰：差有數乎。歧伯曰：又凡三十度也。帝曰：其脈應皆何如。歧伯曰：差同正法，待時而去也。脈要曰：春不沈，夏不弦，冬不濇，秋不數，是謂四塞。沈甚曰病，弦甚曰病，濇甚曰病，數甚曰病，參見曰病，復見曰病，未去而去曰病，去而不去曰病，反者死。故曰：氣之相守司也，如權衡之不得相失也。夫陰陽之氣，清靜則生化治，動則苛疾起，此之謂也。帝曰：幽明何如。歧伯曰：兩陰交盡故曰幽，兩陽合明故曰明，幽明之配，寒暑之異也。帝曰：分至何如。歧伯曰：氣至之謂至，氣分之謂分，至則氣同，分則氣異，所謂天地之正紀也。帝曰：夫子言春秋氣始于前，冬夏氣始于後，余已知之矣。然六氣往復，主歲不常也，其補寫柰何。歧伯曰：上下所主，隨其攸利，正其味，則其要也，左右同法。《大要》曰：少陽之主，先甘後鹹，陽明之主，先辛後酸，太陽之主，先鹹後苦，厥陰之主，先酸後辛，少陰之主，先甘後鹹，太陰之主，先苦後甘，佐以所利，資以所生，是謂得氣。
		The Emperor asked: "Regarding changes in dominance and response (sheng fu), how do they occur early or late?" Qibo said: "When a dominant force (sheng) acts, the condition manifests as disease. Once the disease has developed and becomes evident, the response (fu) begins to emerge." The response (fu) arises after the dominance has reached its peak, and intensifies when it attains its proper position. The degree of dominance varies in subtlety or intensity, while the response may be slight or strong. When dominance is harmonious, the response is also balanced; when dominance is weak, the response reflects that weakness—this is the natural order of Heaven. The Emperor asked: "When dominance and response occur, if their movements do not correspond to the proper positions or arrive late, what is the reason for this?" Qibo said: "The generation and transformation of qi vary in strength and decline." The functions of cold, heat, warmth, and coolness—waxing and waning—are expressed in the four cardinal directions. Therefore, yang's movement begins with warmth and reaches its peak in heat; yin's movement begins with coolness and reaches its height in cold. Spring, summer, autumn, and winter each differ in their respective portions or extents. Therefore, The Da Yao says: "The warmth of spring becomes the heat of summer; the dryness of autumn becomes the cold anger of winter. Carefully examining the four cardinal directions, all signs and omens return to their proper places—thus, one can foresee the end and understand the beginning." This is what it means. The Emperor asked: "Is there a numerical pattern to these differences?" Qibo said: "There are thirty degrees in total." The Emperor asked: "What are the corresponding pulse patterns for these?" Qibo said: "They follow the same fundamental principles, and will naturally subside with time." The Mai Yao says: "In spring the pulse should not be deep; in summer it should not be taut; in winter it should not be 涩 (rough or congested); and in autumn it should not be rapid—this is known as 'four blockages.'" If the pulse is excessively deep, it indicates disease; if excessively taut, it also indicates disease. If 涩 (rough) to an extreme degree or overly rapid, this too signifies illness. When multiple abnormal patterns appear together, it is a sign of disease. When these patterns reappear unexpectedly, it again signals disease. If the pulse pattern should remain but disappears prematurely, or if it should disappear yet persists abnormally, both are signs of disease. Reversals in pulse patterns indicate death. Therefore it is said: "The mutual regulation and balance of qi must be maintained, just as a scale cannot lose its equilibrium." Yin and yang qi, when in tranquility and clarity, promote harmonious transformation and health; but when disturbed or excessive, they give rise to severe diseases—this is what it means. The Emperor asked: "What about the relationship between yin (darkness) and yang (brightness)?" Qibo said: "When two yin forces converge completely, it is called 'yōu' (darkness); when two yang forces unite and illuminate, it is termed 'míng' (brightness). The pairing of yōu and míng corresponds to the differences between cold and heat." The Emperor asked: "What about the solstices and equinoxes?" Qibo said: "When the qi arrives fully, it is called zhi (arrival); when the qi divides equally, it is termed fen (division). At arrival, the qi unifies; at division, the qi differs—this is what is known as the fundamental order of Heaven and Earth." The Emperor said: "You have stated that the qi of spring and autumn begins earlier, while that of winter and summer begins later—I already understand this." Yet the six qi cycle back and forth, and their dominance over each year is not fixed. How then should supplementation and purging be applied? Qibo said: "The treatment of the upper and lower influences should follow what is most beneficial. Correcting their flavors is key; this principle applies equally to the left and right." The Daiyao says: "When Shaoyang is dominant, treatment should begin with sweet and then use salty; when Yangming is dominant, it begins with pungent and follows with sour; when Taiyang is dominant, it starts with salty and ends with bitter; when Jueyin is dominant, it initiates with sour and concludes with pungent; when Shaoyin is dominant, treatment begins with sweet and then uses salty; when Taiyin is dominant, it commences with bitter and finishes with sweet. Support the treatment according to what benefits the condition, and assist based on what generates the qi—this is called attaining harmony with the qi."

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	至真要大... :	
帝曰：善。夫百病之生也，皆生於風寒暑濕燥火，以之化之變也。經言盛者寫之，虛者補之，余錫以方士，而方士用之尚未能十全，余欲令要道必行，桴鼓相應，猶拔刺雪汙，工巧神聖，可得聞乎。歧伯曰：審察病機無失氣宜，此之謂也。帝曰：願聞病機何如。歧伯曰：諸風掉胘1，皆屬於肝。諸寒收引，皆屬於腎。諸氣膹鬱，皆屬於肺。諸濕腫滿，皆屬於脾。諸熱瞀瘛，皆屬於火。諸病痒瘡，皆屬於心。諸厥固泄，皆屬於下。諸痿喘嘔，皆屬於上。諸禁鼓慄，如喪神守，皆屬於火。諸痙項強，皆屬於濕。諸逆衝上，皆屬於火。諸脹腹大，皆屬於熱。諸躁狂越，皆屬於火。諸暴強直，皆屬於風。諸病有聲鼓之如鼓，皆屬於熱。諸病胕腫疼酸驚駭，皆屬於火。諸轉反戾水液渾濁，皆屬於熱。諸病水液澄澈清冷，皆屬於寒。諸嘔吐酸暴注下迫，皆屬於熱。故《大要》曰：謹守病機，各司其屬，有者求之，無者求之，盛者責之，虛者責之，必先五勝，踈其血氣，令其調達，而致和平，此之謂也。
		The Emperor said: "Well said." All kinds of diseases arise from the six pathogenic factors—wind, cold, heat, dampness, dryness, and fire—and result from their transformations. The classics say: "For excess, purge; for deficiency, tonify." I have entrusted this to the physicians, yet even these physicians are unable to achieve perfect results in ten cases. I wish to ensure that essential principles are always applied and produce immediate effects as clear as striking a drum or removing a splinter from skin—such skillful and divine mastery: may I hear about it? Qibo said: "This means carefully examining the disease mechanism without failing to follow the appropriate qi patterns." The Emperor said: "I wish to hear about what constitutes a 'disease mechanism.'" Qibo said: "All conditions involving wind, dizziness, and convulsions are related to the liver." All cold-related symptoms characterized by contraction and pulling are associated with the kidney. All qi stagnation and oppression in the chest are related to the lung. All dampness-related swelling and fullness are associated with the spleen. All heat-related symptoms involving confusion, delirium, and convulsions belong to fire. All itchy sores and eruptions are related to the heart. All symptoms of syncope, retention, and diarrhea belong to the lower jiao. All atrophy, dyspnea, and vomiting are related to the upper jiao. All symptoms of convulsions, shivering, and a loss of mental clarity as if one's spirit has departed belong to fire. All spastic conditions and stiffness in the neck are associated with dampness. All upward-reversing symptoms, such as surging qi or vomiting, belong to fire. All abdominal distension and enlargement are associated with heat. All symptoms of restlessness, mania, or mental derangement belong to fire. All sudden rigidity and stiffness are related to wind. All diseases with a sound like drumming, as if striking a drum, belong to heat. All conditions involving swelling, pain, soreness, and startling or alarming symptoms belong to fire. All symptoms of twisting and contorting the body, as well as turbid bodily fluids, belong to heat. All conditions with clear, cold, and transparent bodily fluids belong to cold. All symptoms of vomiting sour substances or sudden diarrhea with urgency belong to heat. Therefore, the Daiyao says: "Carefully observe the disease mechanism and determine its proper category. Investigate both presence and absence; examine excess as well as deficiency. First understand the five victories (five elements' interactions), clear the blood and qi, and restore their harmonious flow to achieve balance and peace." This is what it means.

1. 胘 : 或作「眩」。《乾隆御覽四庫全書薈要》本作「眩」。

	至真要大... :	
帝曰：善。五味陰陽之用何如。歧伯曰：辛甘發散為陽，酸苦涌泄為陰，鹹味涌泄為陰，淡味滲泄為陽。六者，或收，或散，或緩，或急，或燥，或潤，或耎，或堅，以所利而行之，調其氣，使其平也。帝曰：非調氣而得者，治之柰何。有毒無毒，何先何後，願聞其道。歧伯曰：有毒無毒，所治為主，適大小為制也。帝曰：請言其制。歧伯曰：君一臣二，制之小也，君一臣三佐五，制之中也，君一臣三佐九，制之大也。寒者熱之，熱者寒之，微者逆之，甚者從之，堅者削之，客者除之，勞者溫之，結者散之，留者攻之，燥者濡之，急者緩之，散者收之，損者溫之，逸者行之，驚者平之，上之下之，摩之浴之，薄之劫之，開之發之，適事為故。
		The Emperor said: "Well said." What are the functions of the five flavors in terms of yin and yang? Qibo said: "Pungent and sweet flavors disperse and promote outward movement, belonging to yang; sour and bitter flavors induce vomiting and purging, belonging to yin. Salty flavor also induces vomiting and purging, thus belongs to yin; bland flavor promotes diuresis and elimination, hence belongs to yang." These six flavors either contract or disperse, soften or tighten, dry or moisten, relax or strengthen. They should be applied according to what is beneficial for the condition; regulate the qi and restore balance in this way. The Emperor asked: "For those who do not achieve harmony through regulating the qi, how should they be treated?" What about toxic and non-toxic substances—what is prioritized first or last? I wish to hear your explanation. Qibo said: "Whether a substance is toxic or non-toxic depends on the condition being treated, and dosages are adjusted accordingly." The Emperor said: "Please explain these dosage principles." Qibo said: "One sovereign herb with two minister herbs constitutes a small formula; one sovereign, three ministers, and five assistants form a medium formula; while one sovereign, three ministers, nine assistants constitute a large formula." For cold conditions, use warming therapies; for heat conditions, apply cooling treatments. For mild disorders, treat against the pattern; for severe ones, follow it temporarily. For hardness or solidification, employ reducing methods; for external pathogenic factors, remove them. For exhaustion, provide warmth and tonify; for stagnation, promote dispersion. For retention, use purgative therapies; for dryness, moisten; for tension, relax. For deficiency, warm and supplement; for inactivity, stimulate movement; for shock or fright, calm the spirit. Use upward-moving or downward-moving methods as needed, apply massage or bathing, employ mild or strong interventions, and open or release blockages—adjust according to the specific situation.

打開字典
	至真要大... :	
帝曰：何謂逆從。歧伯曰：逆者正治，從者反治，從少從多，觀其事也。帝曰：反治何謂。歧伯曰：熱因寒用，寒因熱用，塞因塞用，通因通用，必伏其所主，而先其所因，其始則同，其終則異，可使破積，可使潰堅，可使氣和，可使必已。
		The Emperor asked: "What is meant by 'treating against' (ni) and 'treating along with' (cong)?" Qibo said: "Treating against the disease pattern is called 'zheng zhi' (direct treatment), while treating along with it is termed 'fan zhi' (indirect or reverse treatment). Whether to follow slightly or strongly depends on observing the specific condition." The Emperor asked: "What do you mean by 'reverse treatment'?" Qibo said: "Reverse treatment means using heat for conditions that appear hot but are actually cold, and using cold for those appearing cold but truly hot. It also includes treating obstruction with more obstruction and treating diarrhea with more purgation. One must first identify the underlying cause and address it accordingly. Initially, the symptoms may seem similar, but ultimately their treatment differs. This method can break up accumulations, dissolve hardness, harmonize qi, and ensure a definite resolution of illness."

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	至真要大... :	
帝曰：善。氣調而得者何如。歧伯曰：逆之，從之，逆而從之，從而逆之，踈氣令調則其道也。帝曰：善。病之中外何如。歧伯曰：從內之外者調其內，從外之內者治其外，從內之外而盛於外者，先調其內而後治其外，從外之內而盛於內者，先治其外而後調其內，中外不相及，則治主病。
		The Emperor said: "Well said." What is the condition like when harmony is achieved through regulating qi? Qibo said: "Treating against, treating along with, first opposing then following, or first following then opposing—these are all methods. The key is to regulate the qi so that it flows smoothly; this is the principle." The Emperor said: "Well said." What are the differences between internal and external diseases? Qibo said: "For diseases that originate internally and manifest externally, regulate the interior first. For those originating externally but affecting the interior, treat the exterior first. If an internal disease manifests strongly on the exterior, first regulate the interior then treat the exterior. If an external condition affects deeply into the interior, first treat the exterior then regulate the interior. When there is no connection between the interior and exterior, simply address the primary cause of the illness."

打開字典
顯示相似段落
	至真要大... :	
帝曰：善。火熱復惡寒，發熱有如瘧狀，或一日發，或閒數日發，其故何也。歧伯曰：勝復之氣，會遇之時，有多少也。陰氣多而陽氣少，則其發日遠，陽氣多而陰氣少，則其發日近。此勝復相薄盛衰之節，瘧亦同法。帝曰：論言治寒以熱，治熱以寒，而方士不能廢繩墨而更其道也。有病熱者，寒之而熱，有病寒者，熱之而寒，二者皆在新病復起，柰何治。歧伯曰：諸寒之而熱者取之陰，熱之而寒者取之陽，所謂求其屬也。帝曰：善。服寒而反熱，服熱而反寒，其故何也。歧伯曰：治其王氣，是以反也。帝曰：不治王而然者，何也。歧伯曰：悉乎哉問也。不治五味屬也。夫五味入胃，各歸所喜，攻酸先入肝，苦先入心，甘先入脾，辛先入肺，鹹先入腎，久而增氣，物化之常也。氣增而久，夭之由也。
		The Emperor said: "Well said." There is a condition where fire-heat recurs with chills, and fever appears like malaria—sometimes occurring once daily or appearing every few days. What causes this? Qibo said: "This occurs when the alternating forces of excess and recovery meet at a particular time, and their intensity varies." 藟 When yin qi is abundant but yang qi is deficient, the onset of symptoms occurs less frequently; when yang qi is abundant and yin qi is deficient, symptoms appear more often. This reflects the alternating dominance and decline between excess and recovery forces—the pattern of malaria follows the same principle. The Emperor said: "The classics state that cold conditions should be treated with warmth and heat conditions with cooling, yet physicians cannot abandon established principles to change their methods." There are cases of heat illness where cooling treatment results in more heat, and cold conditions where warming therapy causes further cold. Both situations occur when a new illness recurs—how should they be treated? Qibo said: "For conditions where cooling worsens the heat, treat by reinforcing yin; for those where warming aggravates the cold, treat by strengthening yang. This is what is meant by addressing their fundamental nature." The Emperor said: "Well said." Taking cold treatments yet experiencing increased heat, or taking warming therapies but suffering more cold—what causes this? Qibo said: "This occurs because the treatment is applied to a qi that is already in dominance, hence it produces an opposite reaction." The Emperor asked: "If this happens even without treating the dominant qi, what could be the reason?" Qibo said: "What a thorough question!" It is due to not properly applying the five flavors according to their appropriate categories. The five flavors enter the stomach and each goes to where it is most effective: sour enters first into the liver, bitter into the heart, sweet into the spleen, pungent into the lung, and salty into the kidney. Over time, they increase qi—this is a natural transformation of substances. When the effects of these flavors accumulate over time, it may lead to premature aging or illness.
