打開字典
顯示相似段落
	至真要大... :	
帝曰：善。方制君臣何謂也。歧伯曰：主病之謂君，佐君之謂臣，應臣之謂使，非上下三品之謂也。帝曰：三品何謂。歧伯曰：所以明善惡之殊貫也。帝曰：善。病之中外何如。歧伯曰：調氣之方，必別陰陽，定其中外，各守其鄉。內者內治，外者外治，微者調之，其次平之，盛者奪之，汗者下之，寒熱溫涼，衰之以屬，隨其攸利，謹道如法，萬舉萬全，氣血正平，長有天命。帝曰：善。
		The Emperor said: "Well said." What is meant by the composition of formulas in terms of sovereign, minister, assistant herbs? Qibo said: "The herb that primarily addresses the main condition is called the 'sovereign'; those that assist the sovereign are termed 'ministers.' Those that support and guide the ministers to their target are known as 'assistants.' This does not refer to the three categories of superior, intermediate, or inferior herbs." The Emperor asked: "What is meant by these three categories?" Qibo said: "They are used to distinguish the different levels of efficacy and quality among herbs." The Emperor said: "Well said." What is the distinction between internal and external disease patterns? Qibo said: "In prescriptions for regulating qi, one must first differentiate yin and yang, determine whether the condition is internal or external, and treat each according to its proper location." For internal conditions, treat internally; for external ones, treat externally. For mild cases, regulate the condition; for moderate ones, restore balance. For severe excesses, remove or reduce it—use sweating for exterior patterns and purging for interior accumulations. Apply cold, heat, warmth, or coolness according to their respective categories. Follow what is most beneficial, strictly adhere to proper methods, and with every treatment, success will be assured. When qi and blood are in harmony and balance, longevity and a full life span can be achieved. The Emperor said: "Well said." 

75. 著至教論 On Establishing Authoritative Teachings

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	著至教論:	
黃帝坐明堂，召雷公而問之曰：子知醫之道乎。雷公對曰：誦而頗能解，解而未能別，別而未能明，明而未能彰，足以治群僚，不足至侯王。願得受樹天之度，四時陰陽合之，別星辰與日月光，以彰經術，後世益明，上通神農，著至教疑於二皇。帝曰：善。無失之，此皆陰陽表裏上下雌雄相輸應也，而道上知天文，下知地理，中知人事，可以長久，以教眾庶，亦不疑殆，醫道論篇，可傳後世，可以為寶。雷公曰：請受道，諷誦用解。
		The Yellow Emperor sat in the Mingtang hall and summoned Lei Gong, asking him: "Do you know the way of medicine?" Lei Gong replied: "I can recite and understand it to some extent, but I have not yet been able to distinguish clearly; I have not yet attained clarity, nor have I reached the level of making it evident. This is sufficient for treating officials, but insufficient for reaching the level of marquises or kings." I wish to receive the principles of establishing heavenly measurements, harmonizing them with the four seasons and yin-yang; distinguishing stars from the light of sun and moon, in order to clarify the meridian techniques. Future generations will thus become more enlightened, reaching up to Shennong, and establishing authoritative teachings that resolve doubts for the two emperors. The Emperor said: "Good." Do not lose sight of this, for all these are the mutual correspondences between yin and yang, exterior and interior, upper and lower, female and male. One who understands this will know astronomy above, geography below, and human affairs in the middle; such knowledge can endure long-term, be used to teach the common people without doubt or danger. The Discourse on Medical Principles may thus be passed down to future generations as a treasure. Lei Gong said: "Please teach me the Way, and I shall recite it and understand its meaning."

2
打開字典
	著至教論:	
帝曰：子不聞陰陽傳乎。曰：不知。曰：夫三陽天為業，上下無常，合而病至，偏害陰陽。雷公曰：三陽莫當，請聞其解。帝曰：三陽獨至者，是三陽并至，并至如風雨，上為巔疾，下為漏病。外無期，內無正，不中經紀，診無上下，以書別。雷公曰：臣治踈愈，說意而已。帝曰：三陽者，至陽也，積并則為驚，病起疾風，至如礔礰，九竅皆塞，陽氣滂溢，乾嗌喉塞，并於陰，則上下無常，薄為腸澼。此謂三陽直心，坐不得起，臥者便身全，三陽之病。且以知天下何以別陰陽，應四時，合之五行。雷公曰：陽言不別，陰言不理，請起受解，以為至道。帝曰：子若受傳，不知合至道以惑師教，語子至道之要。病傷五藏，筋骨以消，子言不明不別，是世主學盡矣。腎且絕，惋惋，日暮從容不出，人事不殷。
		The Emperor said: "Have you not heard of the transmission of yin-yang?" He replied: "I do not know." The Emperor said: "The three yangs, as heavenly functions, have no fixed patterns in their movements. When they combine, illness arises, and this may cause partial harm to yin or yang." Lei Gong said: "The three yangs cannot be resisted. Please explain further." The Emperor said: "When the three yangs arrive alone, it means all three yangs converge together. When they converge, it is like wind and rain; above it causes dizziness or head diseases, below it results in leaking illnesses." Externally there are no fixed signs, internally there is no regular pattern; they do not conform to the meridians or principles. Diagnosis lacks clear distinctions between upper and lower levels, so it must be differentiated through written records. Lei Gong said: "I have treated cases with sparse success; I merely express my understanding." The Emperor said: "The three yangs are the utmost yang. When they accumulate and converge, they cause sudden startlement; illness arises like a violent wind, arriving as suddenly as thunderclap. All nine orifices become blocked, and yang energy surges excessively, drying the throat and causing obstruction. When it combines with yin, there is no fixed pattern between upper and lower levels; this leads to thin intestinal discharges." This is called the syndrome of three yangs directly affecting the heart: a person sitting cannot rise, and one lying down remains whole-bodied. This is the illness of the three yangs. And through this one can understand how to distinguish yin and yang in the world, correspond them with the four seasons, and harmonize them with the five elements. Lei Gong said: "I do not distinguish clearly in yang matters, nor do I manage well in yin matters. Please rise and explain to me, so that I may grasp the ultimate principle." The Emperor said: "If you receive this transmission but do not understand how to harmonize it with the ultimate Way, you will confuse your teacher's instruction. I shall now explain to you the essentials of the ultimate Way." Disease harms the five zang organs, and tendons and bones are consumed. If you do not speak clearly or distinguish well, then the study of this generation will be exhausted. The kidney is about to fail; there is a sense of sorrow and melancholy. As evening approaches, the person remains composed but does not emerge, showing no earnestness in human affairs. 

76. 示從容論 On Composure and Deliberation

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	示從容論:	
黃帝燕坐召雷公而問之曰：汝受術誦書者，若能覽觀雜學，及於比類，通合道理，為余言子所長，五藏六府，膽胃大小腸脾胞膀胱，腦髓涕唾，哭泣悲哀，水所從行，此皆人之所生，治之過失，子務明之，可以十全，即不能知，為世所怨。雷公曰：臣請誦《脈脛》上下篇甚眾多矣，別異比類，猶未能以十全，又安足以明之。帝曰：子別試通五藏之過，六府之所不和，鍼石之敗，毒藥所宜，湯液滋味，具言其狀，悉言以對，請問不知。雷公曰：肝虛腎虛脾虛，皆令人體重煩冤，當投毒藥刺灸砭石湯液，或已，或不已，願聞其解。帝曰：公何年之長而問之少，余真問以自謬也。吾問子窈冥，子言上下篇以對，何也。夫脾虛浮似肺，腎小浮似脾，肝急沈散似腎，此皆工之所時亂也，然從容得之。若夫三藏土木水參居，此童子之所知問之，何也。
		The Yellow Emperor, sitting in quiet contemplation, summoned Leigong and asked him: "You who have received instruction and recited the texts—if you are able to examine various fields of learning, draw comparisons, and integrate principles into a coherent understanding, then tell me what your strengths are. The five zang organs, the six fu organs—the gallbladder, stomach, large intestine, small intestine, spleen, bladder—brain marrow, nasal mucus, saliva, weeping, crying, sorrow, and grief—all these pertain to the flow of water in the human body; they are all essential for life. If you understand how to treat them properly or recognize mistakes in their treatment, then your knowledge will be complete and perfect. But if you do not know this, you will be blamed by the world." Leigong replied: "I have recited many chapters of the Shang and Xiaopian sections of The Pulse Classic, and I have made distinctions and drawn comparisons; yet I still cannot claim to be perfect in ten out of ten. How could I possibly fully understand these matters?" The Emperor said: "Then try to explain the disorders of the five zang organs and the disharmonies in the six fu organs, the failures in acupuncture and stone therapy, the appropriate use of toxic medicines, the flavors and properties of decoctions—describe their conditions fully. Speak completely and answer clearly; please ask if you do not understand." Leigong said: "Liver deficiency, kidney deficiency, and spleen deficiency all cause heaviness in the body and distress. One should administer toxic medicines, acupuncture, moxibustion, scraping with a stone tool, or decoctions; yet sometimes these treatments are effective and sometimes not. I would like to hear your explanation." The Emperor said: "You, Leigong, have lived many years yet ask few questions. I truly question myself for my own confusion." "I asked you about the profound and subtle aspects of medicine, but you answered with references to the Upper and Lower chapters. Why is that?" "When the spleen is deficient, its pulse appears floating like that of the lungs; when the kidneys are small and weak, their pulse also appears floating like that of the spleen; when the liver is tense and dispersed, its pulse resembles that of the kidneys. These are conditions that even skilled physicians sometimes confuse, yet they can be understood with careful observation." "If it is merely about the three zang organs—earth (spleen), wood (liver), and water (kidney)—and their interrelationships, this is knowledge even a child could be asked. Why then do you ask such questions?"

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	示從容論:	
雷公曰：於此有人，頭痛筋攣骨重怯然少氣，噦噫腹滿，時驚不嗜臥，此何藏之發也。脈浮而弦，切之石堅，不知其解，復問所以三藏者，以知其比類也。帝曰：夫從容之謂也。夫年長則求之於府，年少則求之於經，年壯則求之於藏。今子所言皆失，八風菀熟，五藏消爍，傳邪相受。夫浮而弦者，是腎不足也。沈而石者，是腎氣內著也。怯然少氣者，是水道不行，形氣消索也。欬嗽煩冤者，是腎氣之逆也。一人之氣，病在一藏也。若言三藏俱行，不在法也。雷公曰：於此有人，四支解墯，喘欬血泄，而愚診之，以為傷肺，切脈浮大而緊，愚不敢治，粗工下砭石，病愈多出血，血止身輕，此何物也。帝曰：子所能治，知亦眾多，與此病失矣。譬以鴻飛亦沖於天，夫聖人之治病，循法守度，援物比類，化之冥冥，循上及下，何必守經。今夫脈浮大虛者，是脾氣之外絕，去胃外歸陽明也。夫二火不勝三水，是以脈亂而無常也。四支解墯，此脾精之不行也。喘欬者，是水氣并陽明也。血泄者，脈急血無所行也。若夫以為傷肺者，由失以狂也。不引比類，是知不明也。夫傷肺者，脾氣不守，胃氣不清，經氣不為使，真藏壞決，經脈傍絕，五藏漏泄，不衂則嘔，此二者不相類也。譬如天之無形，地之無理，白與黑相去遠矣。是失吾過矣，以子知之，故不告子，明引比類從容，是以名曰診輕，是謂至道也。
		Leigong said: "There is a person who suffers from headache, muscle cramps, heavy bones, timidity, and shortness of breath; he belches, has abdominal distension, experiences sudden startles, and lacks the desire to lie down. From which zang organ does this condition originate?" "The pulse is floating and taut, and when pressed it feels as hard as stone. I do not know how to interpret this condition; therefore, I ask again about the three zang organs in order to understand their patterns of similarity." The Emperor said: "This is what is meant by careful and composed observation." "When a person is old, one should look to the fu organs for the cause; when young, one should examine the meridians; and when in prime health, one should investigate the zang organs." "Now what you have said is all incorrect. The eight winds have stagnated and matured, the five zang organs are being consumed and scorched, and pathogenic influences are transmitted from one to another." "A floating and taut pulse indicates kidney deficiency." "A deep and hard-as-stone pulse signifies that kidney qi is internally obstructed." 𝑙𝑜𝑎𝑑𝑖𝑛𝑔... 怯然少气者，是水道不通，形体与气息逐渐衰竭的表现。 Coughing, wheezing, and distress are signs of reversed kidney qi. The condition of a single person's vital energy is affected by disease in one zang organ. "To say that all three zang organs are involved simultaneously is not according to the principles." Leigong said: "There is a person whose limbs are weak and drooping, who experiences shortness of breath, coughing, bloody diarrhea. I diagnosed it as lung injury; the pulse was floating, large, and taut. Out of fear, I did not dare to treat him. A crude physician used scraping with stone tools, which caused even more bleeding. When the bleeding stopped, his body felt lighter. What is this condition?" The Emperor said: "You have many skills and knowledge in treatment, yet you have completely missed the nature of this illness." It is like a wild goose flying and reaching the sky. A sage treating illness follows principles and maintains proper limits, drawing analogies from things around him to transform conditions in subtle ways, moving from above to below—why must one strictly adhere to fixed classics? "Now, a floating, large, and vacuous pulse indicates that the spleen qi has become externally cut off, having left the stomach to return outwardly to the Yangming." "Two fires cannot overcome three waters; this is why the pulse becomes disordered and irregular." "Weakness and drooping of the four limbs indicate that spleen essence is not circulating properly." "Shortness of breath and coughing result from water qi combining with the Yangming channel." "Bloody diarrhea occurs when the pulse is tense and blood has no proper pathway to flow." "To think that this condition results from lung injury is a mistaken, even reckless judgment." "Not drawing analogies and comparisons indicates a lack of true understanding." "When the lungs are injured, spleen qi does not remain in its place, stomach qi is unclean, meridian qi fails to function as a messenger, true zang organs deteriorate and rupture, meridians become severed on the sides, the five zang organs leak, and one either nosebleeds or vomits. These two conditions are clearly not similar." "It is like the sky having no form and the earth having no pattern—white and black are vastly different from each other." "This is my mistake. I assumed you already knew, so I did not inform you. Clearly drawing analogies and maintaining composure are essential; this is called 'diagnosis with discernment,' and it is known as the highest principle." 

77. 疏五過論 Shu Wu Guo Lun

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	疏五過論:	
黃帝曰：嗚呼遠哉。閔閔乎若視深淵，若迎浮雲，視深淵尚可測，迎浮雲莫知其際。聖人之術，為萬民式，論裁志意，必有法則，循經守數，按循醫事，為萬民副，故事有五過四德，汝知之乎。雷公避席再拜曰：臣年幼小，蒙愚以惑，不聞五過與四德，比類形名虛引其經，心無所對。
		The Yellow Emperor said: "Alas, how distant it is." Mournful and anxious as if gazing into a deep abyss, or as if welcoming drifting clouds; gazing into the deep abyss is still measurable, but welcoming drifting clouds offers no way to know its boundary. The methods of the sage serve as a model for all the people. When discussing and shaping one's will and intentions, there must be rules and principles to follow. Adhering to established standards and observing medical practices, they act as assistants to the people. Therefore, there are Five Faults and Four Virtues in matters; do you know them? Leigong rose from his seat and bowed twice, saying: "I am young and foolish, confused by ignorance. I have not heard of the Five Faults or the Four Virtues. Comparing categories, forms, names, and falsely citing their principles, my mind has no response."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	疏五過論:	
帝曰：凡未診病者，必問嘗貴後賤，雖不中邪，病從內生，名曰脫營。嘗富後貧，名曰失精，五氣留連，病有所并。醫工診之，不在藏府，不變軀形，診之而疑，不知病名。身體日減，氣虛無精，病深無氣，洒洒然時驚，病深者，以其外耗於衛，內奪於榮。良工所失，不知病情，此亦治之一過也。凡欲診病者，必問飲食居處，暴樂暴苦，始樂後苦，皆傷精氣，精氣竭絕，形體毀沮。暴怒傷陰，暴喜傷陽，厥氣上行，滿脈去形。愚醫治之，不知補寫，不知病情，精華日脫，邪氣乃并，此治之二過也。善為脈者，必以比類奇恒從容知之，為工而不知道，此診之不足貴，此治之三過也。診有三常，必問貴賤，封君敗傷，及欲侯王。故貴脫勢，雖不中邪，精緻神內傷，身必敗亡。始富後貧，雖不傷邪，皮焦筋屈，痿躄為攣。醫不能嚴，不能動神，外為柔弱，亂至失常，病不能移，則醫事不行，此治之四過也。凡風診者，必知終始，有知餘緒，切脈問名，當合男女。離絕菀結，憂恐喜怒，五藏空虛，血氣離守，工不能知，何術之語。嘗富大傷，斬筋絕脈，身體復行，令澤不息。故傷敗結留，薄歸陽，膿積寒炅。粗工治之，亟刺陰陽，身體解散，四支轉筋，死日有期，醫不能明，不問所發，唯言死日，亦為粗工，此治之五過也。
		The Emperor said: "Whenever one is about to diagnose an illness, it is essential to ask whether the patient has previously been noble but later fallen into obscurity. Even if not affected by external pathogens, such a person may develop internal illnesses, which are called 'tuoying.'" Those who were once wealthy but later became poor suffer from what is called "shijing." The Five Qi lingers, and illnesses will coalesce. Medical practitioners diagnosing such cases find no signs in the zang-fu organs, nor any changes in the body's form. When they examine and suspect something, they cannot determine the name of the illness. The body gradually weakens, with Qi deficiency and no essence. When the illness is deep-seated and there is no Qi left, one becomes easily startled and agitated. In such severe cases, the pathogenesis lies in external depletion of Wei (defensive) Qi and internal loss of Ying (nutritive) Qi. A skilled physician's failure to recognize the condition stems from not understanding the nature of the illness; this is also one of the Five Faults in treatment. Whenever one wishes to diagnose an illness, they must inquire about the patient's diet and living conditions. Sudden joy or sudden hardship, initial joy followed by hardship—all of these injure essence and Qi. When essence and Qi are completely exhausted, the physical form is ruined and dispirited. Sudden anger injures Yin; sudden joy injures Yang. When this causes reversal of Qi to surge upward, the meridians become full and the body's form is abandoned. A foolish physician treating such a condition does not know how to tonify or drain, nor does he understand the nature of the illness. As a result, essence and vitality gradually deplete, and pathogenic influences coalesce; this is the second fault in treatment. A skilled practitioner of pulse diagnosis must use analogy and comparison, as well as recognize the extraordinary and constant patterns, to understand it with composure. A physician who practices without understanding these principles is not worthy of respect in diagnosis; this constitutes the third fault in treatment. In diagnosis, there are three constants: one must always inquire about social status—whether noble or obscure—and whether the person has suffered injuries from being a feudal lord or defeated in battle, as well as their aspirations to become a marquis or king. Therefore, when someone of noble status loses power and influence, even without being affected by external pathogens, their essence and spirit suffer internal injury, and the body is inevitably ruined. One who was once wealthy but later becomes poor, even without being harmed by external pathogens, may still suffer from dry skin and contracted tendons, leading to atrophy and spasticity. If a physician is not strict in his practice and cannot stir the spirit, appearing outwardly weak, disorder will arise leading to abnormal conditions. If illness cannot be resolved, then medical treatment fails; this constitutes the fourth fault in treatment. In diagnosing wind-related conditions, one must understand their beginning and end. Knowing the remaining clues, palpating the pulse and inquiring about names should align with the patient's gender. When emotional states such as separation, isolation, depression, and knotting occur, along with sorrow, fear, joy, and anger, the five zang organs become hollow and deficient, and blood and Qi detach from their proper functions. If a physician cannot recognize this, what is the use of discussing medical techniques? Severe injury caused by sudden poverty severs tendons and disrupts meridians, yet the body continues to function 勉强 ly, with vitality persisting but not fully restored. Therefore, injuries that fail to heal properly and stagnate will gradually return to the Yang aspect of the body, resulting in abscesses with alternating cold and heat symptoms. An unskilled physician treating such a condition hastily punctures both Yin and Yang meridians, causing the body to disintegrate and the limbs to twist with cramps. The day of death is then inevitable. If the physician cannot discern the cause, does not inquire into its origin, but merely declares the date of death, he too is an unskilled practitioner; this constitutes the fifth fault in treatment.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	疏五過論:	
凡此五者，皆受術不通，人事不明也。故曰：聖人之治病也，必知天地陰陽四時經紀五藏六府雌雄表裏刺灸砭石毒藥所主，從容人事，以明經道，貴賤貧富，各異品理，問年少長，勇怯之理，審於分部，知病本始，八正九候，診必副矣。治病之道，氣內為寶，循求其理，求之不得，過在表裏。守數據治，無失俞理，能行此術，終身不殆。不知俞理，五藏菀熟，癰發六府。診病不審，是謂失常，謹守此治，與經相明，上經下經，揆度陰陽，奇恒五中，決以明堂，審於終始，可以橫行。
		All these five faults arise from a lack of mastery over medical techniques and ignorance of human affairs. Therefore, it is said: When a sage treats illness, he must understand the principles of Heaven and Earth, Yin and Yang, the four seasons, the regular patterns of the five zang and six fu organs, their distinctions as male or female, exterior or interior, as well as the applications of acupuncture, moxibustion, scraping stones, toxic herbs, and other therapies. He must be composed in dealing with human affairs to clarify meridian pathways. Whether noble or humble, rich or poor, each case has its own characteristics. Inquiring about age—whether young or old—and understanding one's courage or timidity, carefully examining the divisions of the body, knowing the origin and nature of illness, applying the Eight Methods and Nine Conditions of diagnosis—only then will a diagnosis be accurate. The principle of treating illness lies in preserving internal Qi as the most precious asset. One must follow and seek its underlying rationale; if it cannot be found, the fault may lie in misjudging the exterior or interior aspects. Upholding established principles and methods of treatment without deviating from the rationale of acupoints, one who can practice this art will remain free from danger throughout their life. If one does not understand the rationale and locations of acupoints, stagnation may occur in the five zang organs, leading to abscesses forming in the six fu organs. If one fails to carefully examine a diagnosis, it is called losing the normal pattern. Carefully adhering to this method of treatment and aligning with the meridian principles, understanding both upper and lower channels, measuring Yin and Yang, recognizing the five extraordinary conditions, deciding through the Hall of Clarity (mingtang), and thoroughly examining beginning and end—only then can one proceed confidently in medical practice. 


78. 徵四失論 Zheng Sushi Lun: On Four Mistakes

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	徵四失論:	
黃帝在明堂，雷公侍坐。黃帝曰：夫子所通書受事眾多矣，試言得失之意，所以得之，所以失之。雷公對曰：循經受業，皆言十全，其時有過失者，請聞其事解也。帝曰：子年少智未及邪，將言以雜合耶。夫經脈十二，絡脈三百六十五，此皆人之所明知，工之所循用也。所以不十全者，精神不專，志意不理，外內相失，故時疑殆。診不知陰陽逆從之理，此治之一失矣。受師不卒，妄作雜術，謬言為道，更名自功，妄用砭石，後遺身咎，此治之二失也。不適貧富貴賤之居，坐之薄厚，形之寒溫，不適飲食之宜，不別人之勇怯，不知比類，足以自亂，不足以自明，此治之三失也。診病不問其始，憂患飲食之失節，起居之過度，或傷於毒，不先言此，卒持寸口，何病能中，妄言作名，為粗所窮，此治之四失也。是以世人之語者，馳千里之外，不明尺寸之論，診無人事。治數之道，從容之葆，坐持寸口，診不中五脈，百病所起，始以自怨，遺師其咎。是故治不能循理，棄術於市，妄治時愈，愚心自得。嗚呼，窈窈冥冥，熟知其道。道之大者，擬於天地，配於四海，汝不知道之諭受以明為晦。
		The Yellow Emperor was at the Mingtang Hall, and Leigong waited on him while seated. The Yellow Emperor said: "Master, you have studied many texts and received much knowledge. Please try to explain the meaning of gains and losses - what causes gain, and what causes loss." Leigong replied: "Following the classics and receiving instruction, they all say that perfect success can be achieved. However, there are sometimes cases of mistakes or failures; I would like to hear an explanation of such matters." The Emperor said: "Is it that you are still young and your wisdom has not yet matured, or is it that you mix up different ideas in your speech?" The twelve meridians and 365 collateral vessels are all well known to people, and they are what medical practitioners follow and use. The reason why perfect success is not always achieved is because one's spirit and mind are not focused, one's will and intent are disordered, and there is a loss of harmony between the external and internal aspects; therefore, mistakes or doubts sometimes occur. Failure to understand the principles of yin-yang disharmony and reversal is one mistake in treatment. Not completing the study of one's teacher, yet recklessly practicing miscellaneous techniques, falsely claiming erroneous doctrines as the Way, changing names to take credit for achievements, and improperly using acupuncture stones, thus leaving behind consequences that harm oneself - this is the second mistake in treatment. Failing to consider the patient's social status, whether they live in a thin or thick dwelling, their exposure to cold or warmth, failing to adjust diet appropriately, not distinguishing between bold and timid constitutions, and lacking knowledge of analogous cases - all these are sufficient to confuse oneself and insufficient for clear understanding; this is the third mistake in treatment. When diagnosing a disease, if one does not inquire about its origin - such as losses in emotional well-being or dietary imbalances, excessive daily habits, or injuries from toxins - and fails to first address these factors, but instead suddenly takes the pulse at Cun Kou (radial artery), how can one accurately identify the illness? To recklessly assign names to diseases and be exposed to criticism for crude practice is the fourth mistake in treatment. Therefore, what people commonly say about medicine often involves chasing matters far beyond reality, without understanding the principles of measurement and proportion, and conducting diagnoses without proper human consideration. The method of medical treatment requires composure and careful preservation. Simply sitting and taking the pulse at Cun Kou without correctly identifying the five pulses, and failing to understand the origins of a hundred diseases - one begins to blame oneself, yet shifts the fault onto one's teacher. Therefore, when treatment does not follow proper principles, abandoning medical techniques in favor of haphazard practices may sometimes result in temporary improvement, but this is merely the satisfaction of a foolish mind. Alas! How profound and obscure it all is - who truly understands its principles? The great Dao compares to Heaven and Earth, corresponds with the Four Seas. You do not understand that through receiving instruction on the Dao, clarity becomes obscured. 

79. 陰陽類論 Yin-Yang Categories Treatise

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	陰陽類論:	
孟春始至，黃帝燕坐，臨觀八極，正八風之氣，而問雷公曰：陰陽之類，經脈之道，五中所主，何藏最貴。雷公對曰：春，甲乙青，中主肝，治七十二日，是脈之主時，臣以其藏最貴。帝曰：卻念上下經陰陽從容，子所言貴，最其下也。雷公致齋七日，旦復侍坐。帝曰：三陽為經，二陽為維，一陽為游部，此知五藏終始。三陽為表，二陰為裏，一陰至絕作朔晦，卻具合以正其理。雷公曰：受業未能明。帝曰：所謂三陽者，太陽為經，三陽脈至手太陰，弦浮而不沈，決以度，察以心，合之陰陽之論。所謂二陽者，陽明也，至手太陰，弦而沈急不鼓，炅至以病皆死。一陽者，少陽也，至手太陰，上連人迎弦急懸不絕，此少陽之病也，專陰則死。三陰者，六經之所主也，交於太陰，伏鼓不浮，上空志心。二陰至肺，其氣歸膀胱，外連脾胃。一陰獨至，經絕氣浮不鼓，釣而滑。此六脈者，乍陰乍陽，交屬相并，繆通五藏，合於陰陽，先至為主，後至為客。
		At the beginning of spring, the Yellow Emperor sat in quiet contemplation, gazing upon all directions, regulating the eight winds' qi, and asked Leigong: "Among yin-yang categories and meridian pathways, what organ is most precious according to their functions?" Leigong replied: "Spring is associated with Jia and Yi, the green color; it corresponds to the liver in the center, lasts for seventy-two days. This is the time when the pulse is most active. I believe that this organ is the most precious." The Emperor said: "Reflecting on the upper and lower classics regarding yin-yang, what you call most precious is actually the least important." Leigong observed a seven-day period of fasting and purification, then resumed his attendance in the morning. The Emperor said: "Three Yang meridians form the main channels, two Yang meridians serve as collateral pathways, and one Yang meridian functions as a wandering branch. This is how we understand the beginning and end of the five zang organs." Three Yang meridians are external, two Yin meridians are internal; one Yin reaches its extreme at the new and full moons. By fully integrating these, we can establish their principles correctly. Leigong said: "I have not yet understood this teaching clearly." The Emperor said: "What is meant by the three Yang meridians is that Taiyang serves as the main channel. When the three Yang pulses reach Shoutaiyin, they are taut and floating but not sinking. Determine their condition according to measurement standards, examine them with care, and integrate this understanding into yin-yang theory." What is meant by the two Yang meridians refers to Yangming. When it reaches Shoutaiyin, its pulse is taut and deeply urgent without a rebound; if heat reaches this condition, illness will result in death. The one Yang refers to Shaoyang; when it reaches Shoutaiyin and connects upward with Renying, the pulse is taut, tense, and continuous without interruption. This indicates a disease of Shaoyang. If yin dominates exclusively, death will occur. The three Yin meridians are governed by the six channels; they intersect with Taiyin, remaining deep and pulsing without floating upward. This condition reflects a deficiency of willpower in the heart. The two Yin meridians reach the lungs; their qi returns to the bladder, externally connecting with the spleen and stomach. When only one Yin arrives alone, the meridian is cut off; its qi floats without pulsing, appearing as if drawn upward and slippery. These six pulses alternate between yin and yang, intersecting and converging with one another. They interconnect the five zang organs, harmonizing with yin-yang principles; the pulse that arrives first is considered the host, while the one arriving later is regarded as the guest.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	陰陽類論:	
雷公曰：臣悉盡意，受傳經脈，頌得從容之道，以合從容，不知陰陽，不知雌雄。帝曰：三陽為父，二陽為衛，一陽為紀。三陰為母，二陰為雌，一陰為獨使。二陽一陰，陽明主病，不勝一陰，耎而動，九竅皆沈。三陽一陰，太陽脈勝，一陰不能止，內亂五藏，外為驚駭。二陰二陽，病在肺，少陰脈沈，勝肺傷脾，外傷四支。二陰二陽皆交至，病在腎，罵詈妄行，巔疾為狂。二陰一陽，病出於腎，陰氣客遊於心，脘下空竅，堤閉塞不通，四支別離。一陰一陽代絕，此陰氣至心，上下無常，出入不知，喉咽乾燥，病在土脾。二陽三陰，至陰皆在，陰不過陽，陽氣不能止陰，陰陽並絕，浮為血瘕，沈為膿胕。陰陽皆壯，下至陰陽，上合昭昭，下合冥冥，診決死生之期，遂合歲首。
		Leigong said: "I have fully understood the meaning, received and transmitted the meridian teachings, recited to attain the principle of composure, yet I still do not know yin-yang or distinguish between yin and yang as male and female." The Emperor said: "Three Yang is the father, two Yang serves as defense, one Yang establishes order." Three Yin is the mother, two Yin corresponds to yin (female), and one Yin acts independently. When two Yangs combine with one Yin, Yangming dominates the disease; if it cannot overcome this single Yin, it becomes weak and unstable, causing all nine orifices to become sluggish. When three Yangs combine with one Yin, the Taiyang pulse prevails; this single Yin cannot restrain it, leading to internal chaos among the five zang organs and external manifestations of sudden fright. When two Yin combines with two Yang, disease resides in the lungs; if Shaoyin pulse is deeply rooted and overpowering, it harms the lungs and injures the spleen, externally damaging the four limbs. When two Yin and two Yang converge together, disease resides in the kidneys; this results in cursing, reckless behavior, and a head-related illness manifesting as madness. When two Yin combine with one Yang, the disease originates in the kidneys; yin qi lingers and roams within the heart, leaving the lower abdomen's orifices empty. The channels become blocked and obstructed, causing separation of the four limbs from the body. When one Yin and one Yang alternate in absence, yin qi reaches the heart; this causes irregularities between upper and lower parts of the body, loss of awareness regarding entry and exit, dryness in the throat and pharynx, with disease residing in the earth element, i.e., the spleen. When two Yang combine with three Yin, yin reaches its extreme; however, yin does not overpower yang. If yang qi cannot restrain yin, both yin and yang become exhausted. A floating pulse indicates a blood mass (xue jia), while a deep pulse signifies pus or abscesses. When both yin and yang are strong, they descend to the level of yin-yang and ascend to clarity; below, they merge with obscurity. By diagnosing these signs, one can determine the time between life and death, aligning it with the beginning of the year.

3
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	陰陽類論:	
雷公曰：請問短期。黃帝不應。雷公復問。黃帝曰：在經論中。雷公曰：請聞短期。黃帝曰：冬三月之病，病合於陽者，至春正月脈有死徵，皆歸出春。冬三月之病，在理已盡，草與柳葉皆殺，春陰陽皆絕，期在孟春。春三月之病，曰陽殺，陰陽皆絕，期在草乾。夏三月之病，至陰不過十日，陰陽交，期在溓水。秋三月之病，三陽俱起，不治自已。陰陽交合者，立不能坐，坐不能起。三陽獨至，期在石水。二陰獨至，期在盛水。
		Leigong said: "May I ask about short-term prognostications?" The Yellow Emperor did not respond. Leigong asked again. The Yellow Emperor said: "It is discussed in the meridian theories." Leigong said: "I would like to hear about short-term prognostication." The Yellow Emperor said: "Diseases occurring during the three winter months, if they combine with yang, will show death signs in the first month of spring; all such cases are resolved by spring." Diseases from the three winter months have already reached their end in terms of progression; when grass and willow leaves wither, yin and yang both become exhausted by spring. The expected outcome is during the first month of spring. Diseases occurring in the three months of spring are called "yang sha"; when both yin and yang have been exhausted, the expected outcome is at the time when grass dries. Diseases occurring in the three summer months will not last more than ten days if yin is insufficient; when yin and yang intersect, the expected outcome coincides with the time of Qian water. Diseases occurring in the three autumn months involve all three Yang meridians; they will resolve on their own without treatment. When yin and yang intermingle excessively, one is unable to stand when upright, nor able to rise from a seated position. If only the three Yang meridians arrive alone, the expected outcome occurs at the time of Shi water. If only two Yin meridians arrive alone, its expected outcome coincides with Sheng water's peak. 

80. 方盛衰論 Fang Shengshuai Lun

1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	方盛衰論:	
雷公請問氣之多少，何者為逆，何者為從。黃帝荅曰：陽從左，陰從右，老從上，少從下，是以春夏歸陽為生，歸秋冬為死，反之，則歸秋冬為生，是以氣多少逆皆為厥。問曰：有餘者厥耶。荅曰：一上不下，寒厥到膝，少者秋冬死，老者秋冬生。氣上不下，頭痛巔疾，求陽不得，求陰不審，五部隔無徵，若居曠野，若伏空室，緜緜乎屬不滿日。是以少氣之厥，令人妄夢其極至迷。三陽絕，三陰微，是為少氣。是以肺氣虛則使人夢見白物，見人斬血藉藉，得其時則夢見兵戰。腎氣虛，則使人夢見舟船溺人，得其時，則夢伏水中若有畏恐。肝氣虛，則夢見菌香生草，得其時，則夢伏樹下不敢起。心氣虛，則夢救火陽物，得其時，則夢燔灼。脾氣虛，則夢飲食不足，得其時，則夢築垣蓋屋。此皆五藏氣虛，陽氣有餘，陰氣不足。合之五診，調之陰陽，以在經脈。
		Lei Gong asked, "Please ask about the quantity of qi, which is adverse and which is in accordance." The Yellow Emperor replied: "Yang follows the left, Yin follows the right; the old follow above, the young follow below. Therefore, spring and summer belong to Yang as signs of life, while autumn and winter belong to death. If this is reversed, then autumn and winter become signs of life. Thus, whether the amount of qi is excessive or insufficient, if it goes against this principle, it results in 厥 (jue)." Asked: "Is it jue when there is an excess?" Answered: "If qi rises upward but does not descend, cold jue will reach the knees. The young will die in autumn and winter, while the old will live through autumn and winter." When qi rises upward but fails to descend, it causes headache and disease at the crown of the head. One seeks Yang in vain and cannot discern Yin accurately; there are no signs between the five regions (wu bu), as if dwelling in an open wilderness or hiding in an empty room—gradually, one feels incomplete even within a single day. Therefore, jue caused by insufficient qi makes people have delusional dreams and can lead them to the point of confusion and bewilderment. When the three Yangs are exhausted and the three Yins are weak, this is called insufficient qi (shao qi). Therefore, when the lung qi is deficient, it causes people to dream of white objects and see people beheading with blood flowing profusely. When this coincides with its corresponding season, one dreams of battles and warfare. When the kidney qi is deficient, it makes people dream of boats capsizing and people drowning; when this coincides with its season, one dreams of being submerged in water as if filled with fear and dread. When the liver qi is deficient, it causes one to dream of mushrooms, fragrant herbs, or growing grass; when this coincides with its time, one dreams of crouching beneath a tree and being too afraid to rise. When the heart qi is deficient, it causes dreams of rescuing from fire or seeing yang objects; when this coincides with its appropriate time, one dreams of burning and scorching. When the spleen qi is deficient, it causes dreaming about insufficient food and drink; when this coincides with its proper time, one dreams of building walls or constructing houses. These are all caused by deficiency in the five zang organs' qi, with excess Yang qi and insufficient Yin qi. Combine these observations with the Five Diagnostic Methods, regulate Yin and Yang accordingly, and locate them within the meridians.

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	方盛衰論:	
診有十度，度人脈度，藏度，肉度，筋度，俞度。陰陽氣盡，人病自具。脈動無常，散陰頗陽，脈脫不具，診無常行，診必上下，度民君卿，受師不卒，使術不明，不察逆從，是為妄行，持雌失雄，棄陰附陽，不知并合，診故不明，傳之後世，反論自章。至陰虛，天氣絕，至陽盛，地氣不足。陰陽並交，至人之所行。陰陽並交者，陽氣先至，陰氣後至。是以聖人持診之道，先後陰陽而持之，奇恒之勢乃六十首，診合微之事，追陰陽之變，章五中之情，其中之論，取虛實之要，定五度之事，知此乃足以診。是以切陰不得陽，診消亡，得陽不得陰，守學不湛，知左不知右，知右不知左，知上不知下，知先不知後，故治不久。知醜知善，知病知不病，知高知下，知坐知起，知行知止，用之有紀，診道乃具，萬世不殆。起所有餘，知所不足。度事上下，脈事因格。是以形弱氣虛，死。形氣有餘脈氣不足，死。脈氣有餘形氣不足，生。
		There are ten diagnostic standards: measuring a person's pulse (maodu), organ condition (cangdu), flesh (roudu), sinews (jindu), and shu points (yudu). When both Yin and Yang qi are exhausted, the person's illness manifests on its own. When the pulse moves without regularity, Yin is dispersed and Yang is excessive; when the pulse is detached and incomplete, diagnosis lacks consistency. Diagnosis must be conducted from top to bottom. When measuring people of different ranks—commoners, officials, or nobles—if one does not complete training under a teacher, their techniques remain unclear. If they fail to discern what is adverse or in accordance, this is reckless practice: holding onto Yin while losing Yang, abandoning Yin and clinging to Yang, failing to understand the union of both, then diagnosis remains obscure. Transmitting such knowledge to future generations will only result in reversed theories becoming evident on their own. When extreme Yin is deficient, the celestial qi is cut off; when extreme Yang is excessive, the terrestrial qi is insufficient. When Yin and Yang intermingle and intersect, this is the path followed by the perfected person (zhi ren). When Yin and Yang intersect together, Yang qi arrives first, while Yin qi follows later. Therefore, the sage who follows the principles of diagnosis grasps Yin and Yang in their sequence. The extraordinary and constant patterns amount to sixty beginnings (liu shou). Diagnosis combines subtle matters, traces changes in Yin and Yang, clarifies conditions within the five centers, and through this analysis, extracts the essentials of deficiency and excess, establishes the five standards, and understands these things—only then is one sufficiently qualified for diagnosis. Therefore, if one grasps Yin but not Yang, the diagnosis will vanish; if one grasps Yang but not Yin, their learning remains shallow. If one knows the left without knowing the right, or the right without the left, understands above without below, and knows what comes first without understanding what follows—then treatment cannot endure for long. To know ugliness and virtue, to know illness and health, to understand high and low, sitting and standing, movement and stillness—when such knowledge is applied with order, the method of diagnosis becomes complete, and it will remain undiminished for ten thousand generations. Understand where there is excess and recognize where there is deficiency. Measure matters from top to bottom, and pulse conditions arise accordingly. Therefore, when the body is weak and qi is deficient, death occurs. When the form and qi are excessive but pulse qi is insufficient, death occurs. When pulse qi is in excess but form and qi are deficient, life remains.

3
打開字典
	方盛衰論:	
是以診有大方，坐起有常，出入有行，以轉神明，必清必淨，上觀下觀，司八正邪，別五中部，按脈動靜，循尺滑濇，寒溫之意，視其大小，合之病能，逆從以得，復知病名，診可十全，不失人情，故診之或視息視意，故不失條理，道甚明察，故能長久。不知此道，失經絕理，亡言妄期，此謂失道。
		Therefore, in diagnosis there is a great method: sitting and rising follow regular patterns; entering and exiting proceed with order. By turning the spirit and wisdom, one must be clear and pure. Observing both above and below, overseeing the eight correct and incorrect influences (zheng xie), distinguishing the five central regions, examining pulse movement and stillness, tracing the smooth or rough texture of the cun (inch) region, sensing cold or warmth, observing size, correlating with disease manifestations, discerning adverse or in accordance conditions to obtain results, and once again knowing the name of the illness—such diagnosis can achieve tenfold perfection without failing human nature. Therefore, when diagnosing, one observes breathing and intention carefully; thus, order is not lost. The method is profoundly clear and perceptive, hence it can endure for a long time. Those who do not understand this principle lose the meridians and sever reason; they speak falsely and make empty predictions—this is called losing the Dao. 

81. 解精微論 Explanation of Subtle Principles Treatise


1
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	解精微論:	
黃帝在明堂，雷公請曰：臣授業傳之，行教以經論，從容形法，陰陽刺灸，湯藥所滋，行治有賢不肖，未必能十全。若先言悲哀喜怒，燥濕寒暑，陰陽婦女，請問其所以然者，卑賤富貴，人之形體所從，群下通使，臨事以適道術，謹聞命矣。請問有毚愚仆漏之問，不在經者，欲聞其狀。帝曰：大矣。公請問：哭泣而淚不出者，若出而少涕，其故何也。帝曰：在經有也。復問：不知水所從生，涕所從出也。帝曰：若問此者，無益於治也，工之所知，道之所生也。
		The Yellow Emperor was in the Mingtang, and Leigong asked: "I have received instruction and passed it on; I have practiced teaching according to the classics. I understand the principles of form and method, acupuncture for Yin and Yang conditions, moxibustion, herbal decoctions that nourish, and treatment methods. In practice, there are those who are capable and those who are not, so results may not always be perfect." If one first speaks of sorrow, grief, joy, anger, dryness, dampness, cold, and heat, as well as the Yin-Yang differences between men and women, I would like to ask for an explanation of why things are so. Please explain how a person's social status—whether humble or noble—is related to their physical constitution. May all subordinates be guided accordingly; when facing actual cases, one should apply methods in accordance with Daoist principles. I respectfully accept your instruction. I would like to ask about questions that may seem foolish or ignorant, even if they are not covered in the classics; I wish to hear your explanation of such matters. The Yellow Emperor said: "That is a great question." Leigong asked: "When someone weeps but no tears come out, or if tears do appear but there is little nasal discharge, what is the reason for this?" The Yellow Emperor said: "This is mentioned in the classics." Leigong asked again: "I do not know where water originates from, or where nasal discharge comes from." The Yellow Emperor said: "If you ask about this, it will be of no benefit to treatment. It is knowledge that a skilled physician possesses, and it arises from the Dao."

2
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	解精微論:	
夫心者，五藏之專精也，目者，其竅也，華色者，其榮也，是以人有德也，則氣和於目，有亡，憂知於色。是以悲哀則泣下，泣下水所由生。水宗者，積水也，積水者，至陰也，至陰者，腎之精也。宗精之水，所以不出者，是精持之也。輔之裹之，故水不行也。夫水之精為志，火之精為神，水火相感，神志俱悲，是以目之水生也。故諺言曰：心悲名曰志悲，志與心精，共湊於目也。是以俱悲則神氣傳於心，精上不傳於志，而志獨悲，故泣出也。
		The heart is the essence of the five zang organs; the eyes are its orifices. The luster and color of the face reflect its brilliance. Therefore, when a person possesses virtue, their qi harmonizes in the eyes; when they lose it, sorrow becomes evident in their complexion. Therefore, when one is sad or grieved, tears flow—this is where water originates. The source of water refers to accumulated fluid; accumulated fluid belongs to the utmost Yin, and the utmost Yin is the essence of the kidney. The water of fundamental essence does not flow out because it is held in by the essence itself. It is supported and enclosed, so the water cannot move. The essence of water is will; the essence of fire is spirit. When water and fire interact, both spirit and will become sorrowful—thus tears arise from the eyes. Hence the proverb says: "Sorrow of the heart is called sorrow of will; will and the essence of the heart converge together in the eyes." Therefore, when both are sorrowful, the spirit and qi reach the heart, but the essence does not ascend to nourish the will; thus, the will suffers alone, which is why tears flow.

3
打開字典
	解精微論:	
泣涕者，腦也，腦者，陰也，髓者，骨之充也，故腦滲為涕。志者，骨之主也，是以水流而涕從之者，其行類也。夫涕之與泣者，譬如人之兄弟，急則俱死，生則俱生，其志以早悲，是以涕泣俱出而橫行也。夫人涕泣俱出而相從者，所屬之類也。
		Tears and nasal discharge originate from the brain; the brain belongs to Yin, and marrow is what fills the bones. Therefore, when the brain seeps, it becomes nasal discharge. Will is the master of bone; therefore, when water flows and nasal discharge follows, they share a similar nature in their movement. Nasal discharge and tears are like a person's brothers; in urgency, they perish together, and when life is present, they arise together. Since will begins to grieve early on, this is why both nasal discharge and tears emerge simultaneously and flow freely. In people, when nasal discharge and tears flow together in harmony, it reflects their inherent nature and classification.

4
打開字典
顯示相似段落
	解精微論:	
雷公曰：大矣！請問人哭泣而淚不出者，若出而少涕，不從之何也？帝曰：夫泣不出者，哭不悲也；不泣者，神不慈也。神不慈則志不悲，陰陽相持，泣安能獨來？夫志悲者惋，惋則沖陰，沖陰則志去目，志去則神不守精，精神去目，涕泣出也。且子獨不誦不念夫經言乎？厥則目無所見，夫人厥則陽氣并於上，陰氣并於下。陽并於上則火燭光也，陰并於下則足寒，足寒則脹也。夫一水不勝五火，故目眥盲。是以衝風，泣下而不止。夫風之中目也，陽氣內守於精，是火氣燔目，故見風則泣下也。有以比之，夫火疾風生，乃能雨，此之類也。
		Leigong said: "That is great!" I would like to ask, when a person weeps but no tears appear, or if some tears do come but there is little nasal discharge—why does this not follow the pattern? The Yellow Emperor said: "When tears do not flow, it means the crying is not from deep sorrow; those who do not weep at all lack compassion in their spirit." When the spirit lacks compassion, will does not grieve; when Yin and Yang are in stalemate, how can tears come alone? When will is grieved, sorrow arises; when sorrow arises, it strikes the Yin. When Yin is struck, will departs from the eyes; when will departs, spirit no longer guards essence. When both spirit and essence leave the eyes, tears and nasal discharge emerge. And have you not recited or remembered what is said in the classics? When a person experiences jue, their eyes see nothing. When someone experiences jue, Yang qi gathers above and Yin qi accumulates below. When Yang gathers above, it is like a flame illuminating; when Yin accumulates below, the feet become cold, and cold in the feet causes swelling. One measure of water cannot overcome five measures of fire, hence the eyesight fails and vision becomes dim. Therefore, when affected by a strong wind, tears flow continuously without ceasing. When wind attacks the eyes, internal Yang qi protects the essence; however, fire qi burns the eyes, so when exposed to wind, tears flow. This can be compared to how intense heat and strong winds give rise to rain—this is a similar principle.
